# Levers: measured C idioms and blockers

Each lever is one measured finding about how this repo's compilers (gcc 2.6.0 / 2.7.2 with cc1 + maspsx) turn C into the retail bytes. Read them through `python3 tools/scripts/lever.py <keyword>...` (AND of keywords over heading, keywords and text; falls back to OR ranked by hits), `--index` for headings with counts and ids, `--symptoms` for the symptom table.

To add a lever: append a block under the heading that matches what the diff shows, in this exact shape, after you have MEASURED it (a closed match or a probe, with the round, unit and function):

    - id: <area>-<short-name> | keywords: three to eight, lowercase, comma separated
      **Lead sentence naming the construct.** What retail shows, the C spelling that produces it, where it was measured.

Headings are ordered by what a worker SEES in a diff. Each heading has a `symptoms:` line (what the diff looks like) and a `try:` line (words to pass to lever.py).

## register choice / allocator tie

symptoms: swapped registers, wrong s-reg, allocator tie
try: regalloc callee-saved swapped tie
- id: a-second-pointer-alias-carries-the-reads-retail-names | keywords: regalloc, callee-saved, rotation, alias, pointer, copy-propagation, refs, dg
  **WHEN A PARKED BODY ALREADY HAS A SECOND POINTER VARIABLE ASSIGNED FROM THE FIRST (`pTween = pBlock;`, visible in retail as `move s6,s5`) AND THE CALLEE-SAVED HAND-OUT IS ROTATED, THE FIX IS OFTEN WHICH OF THE TWO NAMES CARRIES WHICH FIELD READS - retail's per-site base register in the `.s` (`lh 0xE(s6)` / `lh 0xC(s6)` next to `lh 0xA(s5)`) IS THAT PARTITION, AND MOVING ONE GROUP OF READS BETWEEN THE NAMES CHANGES THE FIRST POINTER'S REF COUNT, WHICH IS WHAT THE HAND-OUT TRACKS** (round 451, battle/main/misc25 func_800B026C, re-parked 95.81 -> 96.08% at retail's EXACT 2120 bytes, 236 -> 192 differing lines). Retail's second pointer carries the interpolation's `startX/Y/Z` group AND the midpoint's `targetZ` test plus both midpoint `targetY` subtractions; the park had put those three on the first pointer. Moving them onto `pTween` alone shifted the block pointer's hand-out from `$s1` to `$s3` (x and y then come out in retail's `$s1`/`$s2`) - a single group move, no byte change. Two limits measured on the same body: cc1 2.6.0 COPY PROPAGATION FOLDS MOST OTHER SPELLINGS BACK (reading the opcode, the absolute-target triple or the tag through the second name is byte-identical, so only a group that survives propagation is a handle - try a group, then check the register actually moved), and `-dg`/`-dl` on that body shows the "regs to allocate" order is NOT monotone in `floor_log2(refs)*refs*size/live` (a 66-ref/276-insn pointer was processed after a 48-ref/160-insn u16 and before a 48-ref/180 one), so the dump tells you where the contested value SITS but not which spelling moves it; measure the group instead. Also measured dead there: s32 x/y/z (95.53, 12 bytes short - the `lhu` loads say the locals are 2-byte), the curFrame/endFrame group moved to the second pointer (95.55), and presets 2.7.2 94.12 / cdk 82.19 / -O3 95.80 against the unit's 2.6.0.
- id: a-two-full-calls-in-if-else-land-the-value-in-a0 | keywords: a0, argument, call, cross-jump, size, select, if-else, ternary, xalloc
  **A VALUE THAT RETAIL COMPUTES STRAIGHT INTO A CALL'S ARGUMENT REGISTER IN BOTH ARMS OF A SELECT IS REACHED BY WRITING TWO COMPLETE IDENTICAL CALLS IN AN if/else, NOT BY ASSIGNING A LOCAL AND PASSING IT - the local lands each arm's result in `$v0` and costs the copy; two calls make gcc compute into `$a0` in each arm and cross-jump the `jal` block** (round 450, field/main/main func_80077884, 90.25 -> 96.38% at retail's exact 560 bytes / 140 insns; combined with the `nStreamSize` hoist the size-select region went from 14 differing lines to 0, which was the whole of residual 2 in a park that had been stuck for rounds). Retail is `bnez v0,<call arm>` / `lui v0,0xFFE2` (in the delay slot) / `lui a0,0xFF` / `ori a0,a0,0xFFFF` / `ori v0,v0,0x3FF8` / `and a0,v1,a0` / `j` / `addu a0,a0,v0` / [call arm] `jal ArchiveDecodeAlignedSize` / `li a0,0x6B9` / `addu a0,v0,zero` / [merge] `jal HeapAlloc` / `li a1,1`: one `jal HeapAlloc` reached by both arms with `$a0` already holding the size, i.e. cross-jumping plus argument-register allocation. Any single `nSize` local, in EITHER if/else order, is 90.25 and gives `and v0,...` / `addu v0,v0,v1` / a copy in the call arm. Two more load-bearing spellings in the same region, each measured: `nStreamSize = g_FieldStreamDataSize;` hoisted above the branch is required for retail's `lw v1,lo(g_FieldStreamDataSize)` above the `bnez` (read inline it sinks into the arm), and the guard/second operand spelling is not what was blocking it. Same mechanism as a-duplicated-guard-load-and-a, reached from the select side.
- id: a-reused-parameter-pseudo-rotates-a-later-loop | keywords: regalloc, rotation, parameter, home-register, counter, loop, fresh-pseudo, temp
  **A LOOP THAT REUSES ONE OF THE FUNCTION'S PARAMETERS AS ITS COUNTER KEEPS THAT PARAMETER'S HOME REGISTER (`$a3`) AND SO ROTATES EVERY OTHER TEMPORARY BY ONE - the fix is a COUNTER VARIABLE OF ITS OWN that dies before the first call, which gcc then hands the LAST temp register retail has it in** (round 449, shop_menu/main/misc ShopMenuSellMenu, 508 insns: a park at 99.73% / 2032 bytes = retail's exact size with the whole six-value hand-out {counter, i, iv1..iv3, pItemTypes} shifted one position, counter in `$a3` where retail has `i`, everything else in the next register up). The six values are allocated to `a3,t0..t4` in priority order, and a parameter pseudo is pinned to its home register while its live range lasts, so `itemType` (the parameter, `$a3` from entry) as the coalesce counter beats every other value and pushes them all up one. Retail's counter is a fresh pseudo in `$t4`, the LAST register, i.e. the LOWEST priority - and two spellings reach that: `coalescedCount` fresh + `itemCount = ++coalescedCount;` gives retail instruction for instruction (this closed the function); folding the two variables into one (`itemCount = 0; ... itemCount++`, or `itemCount` as the subscript index) is 95.95% / 2040 bytes because itemCount stays live across the menu loop's calls, so global-alloc lifts it out of its stack slot into `$s6`, evicting pItemSellQtys and adding 8 bytes of frame. So the rule: when retail holds a loop counter in the last temp register and your body has it in a parameter's home register, do not spell at the counter - split it into a short-lived counter plus the long-lived variable it feeds, and put the copy where retail's per-iteration `sw` is. Measured and dead on the same body (all 2032 bytes unless noted): the whole park note's 15 spellings, two 3-minute permuter runs (base=125 best=125 both), and try_flags 2.7.2 -O2 -G8 97.01 / 2.7.2-cdk 72.55 against the unit's 2.6.0.
- id: a-reset-loop-counter-that-is-a-later-live-variable-fixes-the-saved-map | keywords: regalloc, callee-saved, swapped, loop, counter, reset, tie, cross-jumping
  **A SPRITE/ROW LOOP WHOSE `row` AND `i` LAND MIRRORED IN `$s3`/`$s4` IS USUALLY A RESET LOOP COUNTING WITH A LOCAL `j` INSTEAD OF WITH `row` ITSELF: giving the reset loop `row` makes that pseudo live across the sprite loops' calls, so global-alloc hands it `$s3` and the loop counter of `i` `$s4` - retail's map - and the reset loop's own counter/offset pair comes out in retail's `$s3`/`$v1` instead of a caller-saved `$v1`/`$a0`** (round 447, slus_006.64/system/system func_80034888, 96.83 -> 97.26%, 1564 bytes / 391 insns against retail's 1572 / 393). The reset loop is `for (row = 0; row < pString->maxChars; row++) pString->pChars[row].rowWidth = 0;` with `row = pString->scrollRow;` after it, which also explains retail's reload of scrollRow: the store through `pChars` may alias it. Same mechanism as `a-shared-counter-forces-the-callee-saved-register` (round 405), reached from the reset-loop side. Three measured non-fixes on that seam: a local `j` counter/`i` shared across the loops (96.72/96.83), declaration order of `row`/`i` and per-block scoping of each loop's locals (all byte-identical). The remaining 2-instruction gap on the same body is pure reorg: retail's merge block is `[sb, lhu, andi]` with the `lhu` owning an unfillable nop slot, our sched2 schedules the load first and reorg steals it into the preceding block's `lbu` delay slot, so a `flags = *(volatile u16*)&pString->flags;` local (byte-identical, 97.26) proves a volatile lvalue is NOT a scheduling barrier in 2.7.2.
- id: a-scaled-index-local-buys-the-last-callee-saved-register | keywords: regalloc, callee-saved, index, scaled, stride, rematerialise, cse, struct
  **WHEN RETAIL HOLDS AN `index * sizeof(struct)` BYTE OFFSET IN A CALLEE-SAVED REGISTER ACROSS CALLS AND RE-ADDS IT TO A RELOADED GLOBAL BASE PER STATEMENT, NO PER-STATEMENT `table[i]` BODY CAN REACH IT - cse keeps that multiply in a CALLER-SAVED register (`$a1`) per extended basic block - BUT ONE SOURCE-LEVEL `s32 offset = i * sizeof(T)` LOCAL MAKES gcc USE THE SPARE CALLEE-SAVED REGISTER AND REPRODUCE RETAIL'S SAVED SET AND FRAME EXACTLY** (round 445, field/main/misc2 func_80076AC0, 73.82 -> 76.98%, 1764 -> 1736 bytes / 441 -> 434 insns against retail's 1776 / 444, XenoFieldNoGp). Retail saves s0..s7 + fp (10 words, frame 0x58); the per-statement body saves s0..s7 (9) with the offset in `$a1` and re-forms the `sll/addu/sll/subu/sll` chain per block, so all 423 diff lines are renames. With the offset local used over the whole body: 75.06%, 1692 bytes / 423 insns, gcc takes the 9th callee-saved (`$s1`) and `$fp` for the 7th parameter; with it used only up to the last call of the sprite-setup region (tail left per-statement): 76.98%, 434 insns, saved set and frame retail's, and the head's only register difference is a two-register hand-out (retail spriteId `$s1` / offset `$s2`, we get the mirror). Two further facts measured on the same body: the search is a *count* question first - the body needs 9 callee-saved values and only produces 8, so read the `.mask`/frame slot count in the diff head before hunting renames - and `extern FieldActor* volatile g_FieldActors` (include/field/actor.h) is load-bearing: dropping `volatile` CSEs the base across the statements and collapses the body to 72.15% / 367 insns. Presets dead on the plain body: cdk 66.64, 2.6.0 73.61.
  - id: a-label-alias-array-read-beats-volatile | keywords: store, folding, reload, volatile, alias, array, asm-label, regalloc
  **A LOADABLE RELOAD OF A GLOBAL THE SAME FUNCTION JUST STORED TO (`sh zero,g; ... lh v0,g`) IS KEPT BY READING IT THROUGH A SECOND DECLARATION WITH A DIFFERENT NAME AND AN `__asm__` LABEL, AND THAT SPELLING KEEPS THE WHOLE REGISTER SET WHERE EVERY `volatile` SPELLING MOVES IT** (round 444, world_map/main/misc7 func_800827EC, 96.08 -> 96.25% at retail's exact 240 bytes / 60 insns, XenoWorldMapDiv). Retail stores `g_WorldMapViewRotationY = 0` through the `lui $at` macro and then reads the same halfword back with `lui v0,%hi` + `lh v0,%lo(v0)` + `sll 12`, i.e. the store-to-load fold is blocked but the value is still named at its own symbol. `extern s16 g_WorldMapViewRotation[] __asm__("g_WorldMapViewRotationY");` (declared beside the existing `g_WorldMapCameraRotTable` alias) makes `g_WorldMapViewRotation[0]`/`[1]` a separate gcc object from the stored scalar, so the read survives as `lh`, the relocation is `g_WorldMapViewRotationY+0/+2` instead of an addend on a neighbour global, and the slot pointer stays in `$v1` with the angle in `$a0` as retail has them. Measured on the same body and worse: `volatile s16 *pViewY` store+read 87.98 (a volatile s16 read of a known-0 store becomes `lhu` + `sll 16/sra 4`), volatile store + plain read 93.33, the array read written after both `<< 12` stores 93.17 (the allocator hands the slot pointer `$a1`), and `u32 angleZ` + `v = angleZ` 92.75 - so `volatile` is the trap here and the alias is the lever. Residue after it: the 0x500000 constant sits in `$a0` where retail has `$a1`, one register tie.
- id: a-two-statement-shift-and-mask-is-in-place | keywords: regalloc, srl, andi, in-place, shift, mask, temp, tie
  **RETAIL'S IN-PLACE `srl a3,a0,5 / andi a3,a3,7` (one register for the shift result AND the mask result) IS THE TWO-STATEMENT FORM `x = y >> 5; x &= 7;` - the single expression `(y >> 5) & 7` always leaves the shift in a temp of its own (`srl v0,a0,5 / andi a3,v0,7`), so the temp tells you which spelling the original had** (round 442, slus_006.64/system/rendering func_8001F6B0, XenoRendering, parked 97.38% at retail's exact 160 bytes / 40 insns with the single-expression form). It answers a question about the body but does not buy the match on its own: with the two-statement form the srl/andi pair came out exact and the rest of the caller-saved rotation moved with it (`sll` in place instead of hoisted into a second register, then a `move a0,v1` copy of the loop pointer that retail does not have, counter in `$a1`) - 95.50% / 164 bytes, one instruction over retail. Measured on the same body and dead: declaring the loop's `texPage` inside the loop with an initialiser (byte-identical 97.38 / 160), and `texPageBits = (flags >> 5) & 7;` reading `flags` instead of the dead `color = flags;` copy (byte-identical too - the dead copy is load-bearing at +0.88, see a-dead-store-to-an-unused-local).
- id: a-pointer-local-to-a-scalar-global-always-folds | keywords: regalloc, callee-saved, address, fold, pointer, struct, global, la
  **A POINTER LOCAL BUILT FROM `&<scalar global>` IS FOLDED BACK TO A BARE SYMBOL MEM REF, SO IT CAN NEVER REPRODUCE RETAIL'S "ADDRESS IN `$s0`/`$s1` ACROSS A CALL" - only a pointer whose base object is declared with a STRUCT type survives, and the probe distinguishes the four spellings in ~10 s** (round 442, world_map/main/misc5 func_8007C7D8, parked 74.45% / 1176 bytes against retail's 1172, XenoWorldMapDiv). Retail holds `&g_WorldMapScrollTargetZ` in `$s0` and `&g_WorldMapCameraTargetZ` in `$s1` across two `WorldMapWrapDelta` calls (formed twice, at .L8007C950 and .L8007CA58) while the same function stores `g_WorldMapCameraTarget` through the assembler's `lui $at` / `sw %lo($at)` pair in the same basic block - i.e. retail's source took the address explicitly and the two forms coexist. Measured with `tools/scripts/cc1_probe.sh world_map/main/misc5`: `extern s32 g; s32 *p = &g;` -> folded, bare `lw $2,g`; `s32 *p = (s32 *)&g + 2;` -> ALSO folded, bare `sw $4,g+8` (pointer arithmetic does not block the fold); `extern int g; int *p = &((P *)&g)->z;` -> folded, a cast does not block it; `extern P g; int *p = &g.z;` -> SURVIVES as `la $18,g+8` / `lw 0($18)` / `sw $4,0($18)`, which is retail's exact shape. So when a park says "the address pair is unreachable", first check the DECLARED TYPE of the base symbol: retyping the triple as a 12-byte struct (`extern WorldMapPos g_WorldMapCameraTarget;` covering the x/y/z names) is the route, and it is byte-neutral for the scalar uses (`.x` is the bare symbol, `.z` the symbol+8). Also re-measured on that body and dead: `--gp -G0` 74.45 and `-O3` 74.45 (identical objects - every global here is far from `$gp`, so the -G setting cannot buy the shape) and gcc-2.6.0 does not compile the unit's body at all.

- id: a-store-base-merges-with-the-argument-copy | keywords: regalloc, store, base, argument, copy, local-alloc, fold, a0
  **WHEN RETAIL'S `sw/sb` GO THROUGH THE SAVED COPY BUT YOURS GO THROUGH `$a0` VIA ONE SHARED `move a0,sN`, the two store ADDRESS pseudos merged with the call's argument copy: measured by changing only what the call's argument is, so the compiler cannot see it as the same value (round 437, battle/main/misc32 func_800BDC14, XenoBattleTailCdk0: 91.60% at retail's exact 100 bytes / 25 insns with stores on `$a0` and the `move a0,s0` one slot early, against 97.60 with both stores folded into retail's `136($s0)`/`131($s0)` offset form once the argument was a value the compiler computes). The rejected spelling read the pointer back out of the task's own entry (`pEffect->timerEntry.unk4`), which happens to equal the task there, so it is the same value at runtime and NOT the original code (retail has `move a0,s0`, not a load) - use this as a diagnosis, not as a fix: it says the residual is which pseudo owns $a0 at local-alloc time, i.e. nothing about the store order or the arm's statements. On that body, byte-identical 91.60 and dead: a `u8 *pFlags = (u8 *)pTask + 0x83;` for the flags store (folds back), passing the alias itself as the argument (coalesces), and a `do { } while (0)` around the timer store alone (49.16, it drags the constant into the load-delay slot).
- id: a-one-hand-out-wears-a-whole-clamp-chain | keywords: regalloc, tie, count, clamp, subu, negu, slti, rmw
  **COUNT THE TIES, NOT THE DIFF LINES: a mirrored register on a clamp chain (subu / bgez / negu / slti) or on a flags read-modify-write is ONE pseudo's hand-out carrying every later use, so 22 differing lines can be two ties and both are parkable without a spelling hunt** (round 431, battling/main/misc3 func_80076884, re-parked 99.43% at retail's exact 1972 bytes / 491 insns). Residual 1 is the flags RMW at 0xD0: retail holds the mask in `$v1` and the word in `$a0`, so its `lw 0x648`/`lw 0xFC` land above `sw $a0,0xD0`; ours mirrors ($a0 mask, $v1 word) and the store sinks to just before the `0xFC` load because that load wants the `$v1` the word is still holding - the store POSITION is a consequence of the swap, not a second problem. Residual 2 is one `subu` whose output took the dying subtrahend's `$v0` where retail kept the minuend's `$v1`, and the following `negu` and `slti` ride the same register for free. Also measured on this body and dead: splitting the AND into a local (`newFlags = p->flags & ~0x20000; p->flags = newFlags | ...;`) is byte-identical at 99.43 (the AND result coalesces exactly like a single-use mask local), a second permuter run (1181 iterations, base 145 best 145) matches the first (784, base 145), and the preset screen leaves the unit preset in place (cdk 98.38, 2.6.0 97.88, Default 99.43). So read a multi-line register diff as "how many pseudos are mirrored", and park when each one already has a measured mechanism.
- id: a-flag-word-rmw-mask-can-never-take-v0 | keywords: regalloc, mask, constant, read-modify-write, tie, dl, refs, live
  **ON A FLAG-WORD READ-MODIFY-WRITE THE MASK CONSTANT CAN NEVER TAKE THE REGISTER RETAIL GIVES IT WHEN THE LOADED WORD IS NAMED IN THREE OR MORE INSNS - the `-dl` refs/live numbers prove it in one compile, and this is the reverse of `a-literal-constant-outranks`, where the constant wins because the value it fights is a 1-ref tail** (round 431, slus_006.64/system/temp1 func_80023804, parked 99.10% at retail's exact 312 bytes / 78 insns, XenoGraphics = gcc-2.7.2-cdk -O2 -G8). Retail is `lui v0,0xFFF8 / lw v1,0xAC(t0) / ori v0,0x7F / and v1,v1,v0 / ... / ori v1,v1,0x8000 / sw v1,0xAC(t0)` (mask `$v0`, word `$v1`); every body compiles the mirror. `cc1 -dl` on the live body names the terms: the loaded word is `Register 83 used 6 times across 6 insns in block 0`, the mask `Register 112 used 2 times across 6 insns`, and local-alloc's quotient `floor_log2(n_refs)*n_refs/live_length` is 0.33 for the mask against 2.0 for the word, whose own floor (load, and and store = 3 insns) is 1.0 - still above 0.33 - while on the `n_deaths/n_refs` reading the word's 1/6 is also processed first. The mask could only win with 4 insns mentioning it, which the retail stream does not contain, and a tie still goes to the word because `force_reg` creates the mask's pseudo (112) AFTER the word's (83). One recipe run: `cp build/src/<unit>.c.i build/.dump/u.i && docker run --rm -i --platform=linux/amd64 -v"$PWD":/w -w /w ethos:latest tools/gcc-2.7.2-cdk-psx/cc1 <cc_flags from build.ninja> -O2 -G8 -dl -o /tmp/t.s build/.dump/u.i`, then read `u.i.lreg` from `;; Function <name>`. Measured on the same body and dead, each 99.10 / 312 bytes: the round-417 in-place chain spelling (load into a named local, then `&=`, then `|=`, then one store) - so the chain-on-a-local lever does not reach a mask-vs-word tie - the 12-bit bitfield view, `(0x100 & 0xFFF) << 7` vs `(0x100 << 7)`, constant-first operand order, the mask in a declared local, the loaded-word temp (96.67), an intermediate `v | 0x8000` (93.78), the statement swap (93.37), and presets cdk -G0 96.54, gcc-2.6.0 74.94, gcc-2.7.2 77.18 (cdk -O3 does not compile). Three 3-minute permuter runs: base 70 best 70. The same flip recurs in the unit's func_8002435C (99.19, mask `$v0` / word `$v1` there too), so one park covers the family.
- id: a-shared-const-argument-outranks-a-three-ref-byte-flag | keywords: regalloc, constant, argument, spill, flag, refs, frame
  **A SHARED `CONST_INT` USED AS A STACK ARGUMENT AT FOUR CALL SITES TAKES A CALLEE-SAVED REGISTER FROM A 3-REF BYTE FLAG, AND NO SPELLING OF EITHER SIDE FLIPS IT - the flag spills instead, which costs one extra local slot and 8 bytes of frame** (round 433, menu/main/misc9 func_801DDF24, parked 94.46% at retail's exact 884 bytes / 221 insns, XenoMenu260 = gcc-2.6.0). Retail keeps `needsOpen` in `$s5` and rematerialises the a6 `1` (`li t0,1` + `sw t0,0x18(sp)`) at each of four `MenuCreateWindow` calls; every body keeps the constant (`li s8,1`, `sw s8,0x18(sp)`) and spills the flag (`sb t0,0x38(sp)` / `lbu v0,0x38(sp)`), so the frame is 0x70 against retail's 0x68 with all 9 callee-saved registers identical in count and the whole diff a rotation. The four literals fold to ONE pseudo whatever the source says (a named `u_char border = 1` local, a preheader `s32 mode = 4` for the sibling a7 constant: 93.80 / 888), so this is `a-literal-constant-outranks` seen from the variable's side: the flag would need 8+ refs, i.e. instructions retail does not have. Park on the tie and record which local slot is the surplus one. Also measured dead on the same body: `s32 kind` with `kind = arg2 & 0xFF` (reproduces retail's `lw` compare but 90.28 / 892), the func_801DCE60 prototype flip `s32 -> u_char` (+0.32%, retail masks `a1` at the call site), a 4-minute permuter run (base 4368 best 270) whose winner passes the flag where retail passes 0 - wrong code, and presets gcc-2.7.2 94.81 / 2.6.0 94.46 while the unit cannot switch (six matched functions drop to 96-98 under 2.7.2, one to 67.27). ROUND 446 confirmed it with the allocator dump and closed the preset axis: on  menu/main/misc9 func_801DDF24's parked body `cc1 -da -dg` prints `;; 16 regs to allocate: 149 147 159 84 78 80 75 79 97 81 103 102 95 77 76 73` with dispositions `103 in 23` (the a7 `4`) and `102 in 30` (the a6 `1`, whose RTL is `(set (reg:SI 30 $fp) (const_int 1))` with `REG_EQUIV (const_int 1)` - gcc knows it is rematerialisable and still spends a callee-saved register, because loop.c hoisted it), while the flag's allocno gets no disposition at all. gcc-2.7.2 is NOT a way out: its object for the same body has the identical `li s8,1` / `li s7,4` pair and the same spilled flag (0x70 frame against retail's 0x68), so the 0.35-point 2.7.2 score is the same residual, not a different one (cdk 86.95, -O3 67.90).
- id: a-address-pointer-formed-once-before-a-switch-buys-the-third-saved-register | keywords: regalloc, callee-saved, address, rematerialise, pointer, switch, prologue, frame
  **A `&global` USED IN THREE PLACES INSIDE ONE SWITCH ARM IS REMATERIALISED BY gcc (lui+addiu per use, no saved register), WHILE THE SAME POINTER FORMED ONCE IN THE BLOCK BEFORE THE SWITCH KEEPS A CALLEE-SAVED REGISTER AND REPRODUCES RETAIL'S PROLOGUE - retail's saved set is the evidence of which shape the original had** (round 430, world_map/main/misc7 func_800828DC, parked 75.62%, XenoWorldMapDiv preset). Retail saves ra/s2/s1/s0 and holds `&g_WorldMapScrollTargetX` in `$s0` from the middle of case 11 to the `WorldMapWrapPosition` call; the pointer assigned inside the arm scores 72.19 with two saved registers (the address is re-formed at the load, at the store through `$at`, and again for the call), a do/while around the arm's uses does not stop it (73.04), and an initialiser at the top of the function keeps a register but the pseudo then dies last and takes `$s2` (74.10). Forming it once between the last pre-switch call and the `switch` gives exactly retail's three saved registers and its `sw ra/0x1C, s2/0x18, s1/0x14, s0/0x10` prologue. Which of the three gets `$s0` is still global.c's refs/live-length quotient (pSlot then takes `$s0` here; retail's pointer did), so treat the saved-register COUNT and prologue shape as the reachable part and the permutation as the park.
- id: a-local-alloc-call-crossing-qty-takes-the-first-callee-saved | keywords: regalloc, local-alloc, callee-saved, block-local, dg, dl, crosses-calls, unreachable
  **A BLOCK-LOCAL QUANTITY THAT CROSSES A CALL IS GIVEN THE FIRST FREE CALLEE-SAVED REGISTER BY local-alloc, WHICH RUNS BEFORE global-alloc, SO WHEN RETAIL HAS A *GLOBAL* ALLOCNO IN `$s0` AND A BLOCK-LOCAL IN `$s1` THE PAIR CANNOT BE REACHED BY ANY SPELLING - one `-dl -dg` compile proves it** (round 4xx, battling/main/misc10 func_80084C88, parked 97.37% at retail's exact 840 bytes / 210 insns, Default preset, whole body instruction-identical with pActor `$s1`/pNode `$s0` mirrored). The dump separates the two classes in one look: `-dl` prints "Register 77 used 3 times across 10 insns in block 3; crosses 2 calls" (pNode - the only block-3 qty that needs a callee-saved register, forces `sw s0` on the frame) and `-dg` prints "12 regs to allocate: 85 83 82 72 86 84 81 109 80 73 76 74" with "72 in 17" for pActor ("used 41 times across 107 insns; dies in 0 places; crosses 9 calls"). Because local-alloc ran first, pActor - 4th in that order and the first of the twelve to need a callee-saved register - gets `$17`, never `$16`. So the question to ask of such a park is not "which priority wins" but "what else in that block needed a callee-saved register", and the code answers it: every other block-3 qty is caller-saved (79 "crosses 1 call" but dies at that call, the rest fully local). The one shape that adds such a qty - making pObj live across the append call by moving its store after it - costs the store position (95.10% / 852 bytes). Recipe in one container run: `cp build/src/<unit>.c.i /tmp/m.i && tools/gcc-2.7.2-psx/cc1 <unit cc_flags> -O2 -G8 -dl -dg -o /tmp/m.s /tmp/m.i`, then awk the section between `;; Function <name>` and the next `;; Function` out of `/tmp/m.i.lreg` and `/tmp/m.i.greg` (dump per-pseudo refs, then `Register dispositions` and `Hard regs used`). Presets screened on the same body and dead: gcc-2.7.2-cdk 94.04, gcc-2.6.0 96.13.
- id: a-duplicate-walker-local-costs-a-callee-saved-register | keywords: regalloc, callee-saved, spill, walker, cursor, coalesce, parameter, cascade
  **A `pSprite = pSprites;` COPY THAT WALKS THE SAME ARRAY THE PARAMETER NAMES COSTS A WHOLE CALLEE-SAVED REGISTER AND CASCADES INTO TWO SPILLED LOCALS: keep the parameter as the cursor (`pSprites++`) and the copy coalesces** (round 427, battle/main/misc21 func_800A2704, 77.19 -> 79.84 -> 80.86%, 1036 -> 996 bytes / 259 -> 249 insns against retail's 968 / 242, XenoBattle260Div). The parameter `pSprites` was dead after the preheader, but gcc kept it in `$s7` and only handed it to the cursor in `$s1` at the loop entry (`move a0,s7` / `addiu s1,a0,116`), so the hoisted constant 1 took `$s7` and both `flagA` and `endFrame` were spilled and reloaded per use (7 of the 17 surplus instructions). With one value retail's shape appears: `addiu s1,s1,116` once, then `-4(s1)`/`0(s1)` and `addiu s1,s1,124` in the tail delay slot. Companion measurement in the same body with the same mechanism: moving `flagA = flags & 1;` from the first statement after the early return down to just before its test (retail's own order, after the countA/limit/countB block) was worth 77.19 -> 79.84 on its own, and `u8 flagA` (80.44 / 1000 / 250) and `s32 flagA` (no change) are dead on top. Different in kind from the allocno-permutation parks around it: here the surplus was the spill cascade, so count the loads of a spill slot in the diff before blaming the register rotation.
- id: a-whole-caller-saved-rotation-in-a-counting-loop | keywords: regalloc, rotation, caller-saved, count, loop, declaration-order, 260
  **A THREE-VALUE ROTATION ACROSS `$a0`-`$a2` IN A LOOP THAT COUNTS (`count`, the `i*stride` temp and the row pointer) IS THE 2.6.0-vs-2.7.2 ALLOCATOR ORDER, NOT A SPELLING: 2.6.0 hands registers out in allocno (declaration) order - count `$a0`, temp `$a1`, p `$a2` - and 2.7.2's priority sort gives the temp `$a0` and count `$a2`, which drags every downstream register and two callee-saved with it** (round 426, battle/main/misc19 func_8009B46C, parked 97.59% at retail's exact 536 bytes / 134 insns, Default preset; the same body is 97.81 under gcc-2.6.0). The rotation is what the diff shows first (all 108 differing lines), so before hunting it: two declaration-order probes measured BYTE-IDENTICAL - `i` declared before `count`, and the two `u32 max/cur` hoisted from the loop body to function scope - and the three `mflo` read-backs (`mflo v1` / `mflo v0` vs `mflo t1`) that remain are the round-373 md wall. A unit whose matched functions all stay 100 under 2.6.0 is the only fix: screen with `try_flags.sh <unit> --all --gcc gcc-2.6.0-psx` before spending a lane on these.
- id: a-single-block-function-skips-global-alloc | keywords: regalloc, tie, dg, dl, dispositions, single-block, local-alloc, leaf
  **A LEAF FUNCTION WITH ONE BASIC BLOCK NEVER REACHES global_alloc (its dump has the `Register dispositions` / `Hard regs used` block but NO `N regs to allocate` line), so `-dg` has nothing to say and the `-dl` dispositions plus each pseudo's `used N times across M insns` line are the whole evidence - and they name the mirror directly** (round 420, world_map/main/misc6 func_8007E450, parked 97.97% at retail's exact 148 bytes / 37 insns, XenoWorldMapDiv, whole body instruction-identical with only pActor/posZ swapped between `$v1`/`$a2`). The dump `73 in 6 75 in 4 76 in 6 77 in 4 78 in 5 79 in 3` maps by pseudo number: 73 = the actor pointer (`reg/v:SI`, the `plus` of the `lw g_WorldMapSlots` 76 which addsi3 ties to it, and the `index << 7` 75), 77/78/79 = the three position value webs. Retail's `$v1`/`$a0`/`$a1`/`$a2` is the ascending-n_refs hand-out (constant 80 first, then 73 with 7 uses, then 77/78/79), i.e. the order local-alloc would use - our compile gave 79 (10 uses) the register before 73, which is a quantity-set difference (the two stores plus posZ's REG_DEP_ANTI refs) and not a spelling of that body. Read one `-dl` before parking on a "two registers swapped, everything else identical" park note in a leaf; also measured on the same body and dead: the values as initialised declarations inside the do-block (96.62), the chained `g = h = *(u32 *)global;` store form (85.68), and dropping the load alias (96.62).
- id: a-scheduler-carries-the-mask-register | keywords: regalloc, mask, li, sched, dl, prologue, tie, live
  **WHEN A PROLOGUE'S ONLY RESIDUAL IS A FORCED-CONSTANT MASK IN THE WRONG REGISTER PLUS THE TWO LOADED COORDINATES IN THE WRONG ONES, THE SCHEDULER HAS ALREADY DECIDED IT: `-fno-schedule-insns/-insns2` (a DIAGNOSTIC, not a preset flag) hands back retail's mask/AND registers, which proves no statement order can, and the `-dl` refs/live numbers prove the mask cannot win on priority either** (round 422, battling/main/misc9 func_80082488, parked 99.00% at retail's exact 1016 bytes / 252 insns, XenoBattlingNoGp). Retail is `move s1,a0 / li a0,-0x100 / sw ra` then `lw t2,0(s1)`/`lw t1,8(s1)`/`and a2,t2,a0`/`sra t2,t2,8`/`and a0,t1,a0`/`sra t1,t1,8` - mask `$4`, x `$10`, z `$9`, both shifts in place - and the scheduled build emits `li a3,-0x100`, x `$2`, z `$4`, col `$10`, row `$9` with the second `and` SUNK below the index chain. The same `.i` with `-fno-schedule-insns -fno-schedule-insns2` moves the second `and` back beside the first and gives the mask `$4`, col `$10` and row `$9` (retail's registers for three of five), but reorders the prologue saves, so it is evidence rather than a fix. `-dl` on the body: pseudo 83 (the forced -256 constant) 3 refs / 28 insns, 80 (x) 3/5, 81 (z) 3/12, 78 (mx) 4/23, 79 (mz) 4/12 - the mask has the smallest refs/live quotient, so it cannot outrank x for `$2`; `-dg` shows only 23 global allocnos and none of these five (all block-local). Also dead on this body: six order/type/idiom permutations of the assignments (byte-identical 99.00), gcc-2.6.0 97.70, cdk 89.19, and a 1393-iteration permuter run (base 255 best 190, the only find drops the mask = wrong code). So on a prologue whose diff is a mask constant's register, run the no-schedule compile FIRST: if it restores the registers, park on it instead of permuting.
- id: a-call-free-single-block-temp-in-t0-is-a-compiler-trait | keywords: regalloc, t0, t1, v0, temp, local-alloc, leaf, copy
  **A LEAF, CALL-FREE, ONE-BLOCK BODY WHOSE TWO SHORT-LIVED LOAD TEMPS RETAIL KEEPS IN `$t0`/`$t1` IS A COMPILER-BUILD TRAIT, NOT A SPELLING: check how many functions in the UNIT show it before spending a lane** (round 429, battling/main/misc14 func_8008BB00, parked 94.29% at retail's exact 28 bytes / 7 insns, Default preset; the whole body is instruction-identical and only the two temps are named differently). Such a body never reaches global_alloc, so local-alloc's scan hands the QTYs the first free registers, and every compiler in `tools/` starts at `$2` - `gcc-2.7.2` -O2/-O3/-O1/-G8/-G0, `2.7.2-cdk` and `2.6.0` all give `$v0`/`$v1`, so the scan start is not reachable from source. The cheap screen for this class is `grep -l 'lui \$t0' asm/<bin>/nonmatchings/<unit>/*.s`: when the ONLY hits in the unit are the stub and its sibling (as here, func_8008BB1C, the same two-word copy idiom — one `lw $t0,0($a0)` and no address pseudo at all), it is the retail compiler's REG_ALLOC_ORDER and the park is closed; when most of the unit's originals use `$tN` temps, suspect the unit's preset instead.
  Round 448 extended it to the ADDRESS pseudo of a lone store: slus_006.64/system/archive_b func_800379B4 (parked 96.00%, 20 bytes = retail's exact 5 insns, only `lui/addiu` going to `$t0` where we get `$v0`) is the ONLY `lui $t0` in the whole unit's asm, and `cc1_probe.sh` on six further spellings (`*A`, value copied through a local, a struct-typed `__asm__` alias read through a pointer local, a pointer local over the volatile array, `p[0]`, a `mem_addr` cast) gives `la $2` for the four that keep the three-instruction form and folds the other two to the bare symbol macro (16 bytes), so neither the address nor the value pseudo can be moved; the unit's NEXT glabel being a hand-written 8-byte `j` thunk says the region is not our cc1's output.
- id: a-block-local-address-pair-is-one-rotation-step | keywords: regalloc, tie, local-alloc, block-local, rotation, pointer, address
  **TWO `&local` ADDRESS PSEUDOS INSIDE ONE BASIC BLOCK ARE BLOCK-LOCAL QUANTITIES, so which of them takes the lower callee-saved register is local-alloc's rotating scan start (`find_free_reg` begins where the previous allocation in that block stopped), NOT global-alloc's refs/live quotient - read the `-da` line for `in block N` before spending a lane on the priority formula** (round 419, battling/main/misc4 func_80078920, parked 98.11% at retail's EXACT 1024 bytes / 256 insns, Default preset, whole body instruction-identical and 28 differing lines of register naming only). Retail keeps `&edge1` in `$s6` and `&edge2` in `$s7` and then reuses the dying `$s6` for both the first distance result and the ratan2 angle; the compile mirrors the pair, puts the distance in `$s0` and the angle in `$s3`, and every downstream difference (the deferred `move s0,v0`, the angle copy in the next call's delay slot) follows from that one swap. The `-da` `.lreg` dump gives the two addresses as `used 3 times across 36 insns in block 9; crosses 1 call; pointer` and `used 3 times across 28 insns in block 9; crosses 1 call` - the same refs, and block-local, so the quotient is not the mechanism; one rotation step decides, and block 9's instruction sequence is already retail's exactly, so nothing spellable shifts it (naming the addresses `Vec4* pE1 = &edge1;` compiles byte-identically, gcc coalesces them). Two permuter runs from this body were already recorded (base 430 -> best 230, the hoist). Park after one `-da` look whenever a park note blames the two address allocnos tying on refs.
  **SOLVED in round 433 (99.45 -> 100.00, 1024 bytes / 255 insns, Default preset): the swap was NOT the address pair - it was a SECOND C VARIABLE the original body did not have.** A third permuter run from the 98.11 body came back `PERMUTER_BETTER base=340 best=140` with the single edit `edge2.z -= (angle = q->prevPosition.z);`, i.e. a DEAD STORE that starts `angle`'s live range back in the copy block; porting it measured 99.45 and left only the `$s6`/`$s7` pair mirrored, and a fourth run from that body was `base=140 best=140` (NONE, 1216 iterations). The dead store was the permuter's way of saying "retail's own variable was born here": deleting the `angle` local and REUSING `dist0` for the angle (`dist0 = ratan2(...)`, `rcos(dist0)` x2, `rsin(dist0)` x2) gives retail EXACTLY - `$s6` holds `&edge1`, then `dist0`, then the angle, in that order, because the one pseudo is born at the first distance call instead of after the second. So when a permuter's best candidate is a dead store into a variable that is live later, do not ship the store: ask which two C variables of yours retail could have spelled as one, merge them, and the "local-alloc rotation" usually disappears. (Same lesson from the other side: adding a shared pointer variable to func_8007AC3C is byte-identical, because a copy of an ADDRESS coalesces into the per-copy pseudo - only a value that is a real computation survives as one allocno.)
Round 432 confirmed the same verdict on a caller-saved pair with IDENTICAL `-dl` numbers: battle/main/misc16_r2 func_80090310 is 98.70% at retail's exact 400 bytes / 100 insns with only `$v0`/`$v1` mirrored (the `lw g_BattlePrimStartIndex` result vs its `*5` product), and `cc1 -dl` prints `Register 89 used 2 times across 3 insns in block 1` for one and `Register 90 used 2 times across 3 insns in block 1` for the other - equal refs, equal live length, so there is no priority to win and the register a body's arms get is qty-scan order (a 1618-iteration permuter run agrees: base 130 best 130). Measured dead on that body: a `u16 pal` local before each arm's store (85.09 / 94 insns, it stops the arms being cross-jump-mergeable) and a per-arm `ofs` local (91.35 / 104).
- id: a-no-callee-saved-tie-has-no-dump | keywords: regalloc, tie, block-local, qty, caller-saved, mode, sum, dg
  **A FUNCTION WITH NO CALLEE-SAVED REGISTER HAS NO ALLOCATION QUESTION FOR `-dl`/`-da` TO ANSWER: the tie is between BLOCK-LOCAL caller-saved quantities, and the pseudo's MODE SIZE (the `allocno_size` term) does not move it** (round 419, world_map/main/misc13 func_80094154, parked 98.21% at retail's exact 112 bytes / 28 insns, XenoWorldMapDiv). The whole body is instruction-identical except that retail ties each `subu` dest to its MINUEND and takes the two `mflo` results in `$a2`/`$v1` where the compile mirrors them into `$a3`/`$v0` - the same six registers (`v0 v1 a0-a3`), permuted, in a function whose frame saves only `ra`. Because the `-dl`/-da dumps exist to decide which pseudo wins a CALLEE-saved register, and there is none here, the a-global-allocno-priority-is / a-lreg-dump-gives-the recipe has nothing to report: don't spend the compile. One spelling measured on the way that changes an allocno's size and settles that axis: declaring the accumulator `u32 sum` instead of `s32 sum` emits BYTE-IDENTICAL code (98.21, 112 bytes, 28 insns), so the sum's mode is not part of the contest; the parked note already covers the other seven spellings tried (single-expression sums 72.86, separate square locals 79.46, reused single `d` local, split statements 91.79) and a 2193-iteration permuter run that found only a duplicated `negu`.
  Round 433 measured the split/in-place family on the same body (all 112 bytes / 28 insns): `dx = p0->x; dx -= p1->x;` alone is 91.79 (cc1 hoists one `sra` into the `bgez` delay slot and duplicates it); the same split plus `dx >>= 12; sum = dx*dx;` is 95.00 and DOES give retail's `subu` tie (dest = the minuend's register) and retail's whole `bgez/nop/negu/sra/mult` chain, but the load/dest pair moves to `$v1` (retail `$v0`) and the z half to `$a0`/`$v0`, so the mirror only relocates; and `p1->x - p0->x` (identical under the abs) costs a `move a2,a0` parameter copy, 116 bytes / 29 insns. The constant form's mirrored tie and the in-place form's mirrored pair are exclusive - park the 98.21 constant form.
- id: a-store-order-chooses-the-li-hoist | keywords: li, scheduler, store, order, hoist, sched1, absolute
  **IN A BLOCK OF TWO-DOZEN ABSOLUTE-ADDRESS IMMEDIATE STORES, cc1's SCHEDULER ONLY REORDERS THE STORES (and so only hoists the `li`s above them, and so only gives the constants separate registers) WHEN THE SOURCE ORDER INTERLEAVES A CONSTANT GROUP WITH ITS NEIGHBOUR - putting the groups in the order the `li`s come out produces source-order stores and reuses one register** (round 433, world_map/main/misc13 func_80092DF8, 91.17 -> 97.97 at retail's exact 480 bytes / 120 insns, XenoWorldMapDiv). Retail emits stores C2,CA,C8,D8,C0,D0 (two stores per constant) with `li t0,0x98` five insns above its first store and each constant in its own register; writing the source in exactly retail's `li` order (the whole 0x98 pair before the 0x70 pair) measures 95.76 because cc1 then emits the stores in source order and reuses `$v0` for every constant, while the interleaved order `C2,C0,CA,C8,D8,D0` makes it reorder the C0 store down into retail's slot and hoist its `li` (97.97, residual = two scheduler slots and two register names). So when a big constant-store block's residual is `li` positions plus a reused register, permute the STATEMENT order of the pairs; the store order in the diff is the scheduler's output, not the source. Same round: the same body's flags read-modify-write needs the address materialised once (`la a1` + `lhu`/`sh` through it) and only an ARRAY-typed declaration gets that (a `u16*` local is folded back by cse) - its 91.17 -> 96.26 leg.
- id: a-def-order-probe-separates-a-pinned-tie | keywords: regalloc, tie, local-alloc, priority, refs, live, def-order, probe
  **MOVING A PRODUCER'S DEF ABOVE AN UNRELATED ONE-SHOT LOAD IS THE ONE CHEAP PROBE THAT SAYS WHETHER A CALLER-SAVED TIE IS ORDER-DRIVEN OR PINNED: if the register does not move, neither local-alloc's refs/live priority nor its qty order is what assigns it, and no further spelling is worth a diff run** (round 419, slus_006.64/psyq/libgpu_c func_80046F30, parked 99.51% at retail's EXACT 364 bytes / 91 insns, PSY-Q preset). Retail's GPU-timeout panic block holds the `g_GpuGp1Port` pointer in `$v1` and the `g_GpuQueueHead` value in `$t0`; the body puts the pointer in `$a2` and the head in `$v1` and every other instruction, the frame and the saved set are exact. The `-dl` dump of this body gives the head 2 refs over 3 insns and the pointer 3 refs over 22, so the head's refs/live quotient is ~5x the pointer's and it wins the first free caller-saved register - the natural reading is "the head is allocated first, so make the pointer's qty older". Doing that (`head = g_GpuQueueHead;` moved above the `(void)*g_GpuGp1Port;` status read) measures 94.66% / 30 differing lines and the pointer is STILL `$a2`, which rules out both order theories and makes the assignment a pinned allocator artifact: park on the -dl numbers plus this probe instead of spending a lane. Also identical at 99.51 / 14 lines on this body: `u_long queued`, the head as its own two-statement local, a separate `volatile u_long *statusPort` local for the dead read (cc1 CSEs it back to the single pointer load), -O2, gcc-2.6.0, and a 6th permuter run (1420 iterations, base=45 best=45, NONE); gcc-2.7.2-cdk is 65.03.
Round 419 re-ran the same probe on a CALLER-saved leg with the same verdict: battle/main/misc31 func_800BB350 (parked 98.10% at retail's exact 496 bytes, XenoBattleTailCdk0), where the `(flags & 0x3FFFFFFF) | (slot << 30)` read-modify-write needs retail's shift->$v0 / mask->$v1 / load->$a0 and gets load->$v0 / mask->$v1 / shift->$a0. Giving the shift its own `u32 slotBits = slot << 30;` statement ABOVE the flags load (i.e. moving its def first) leaves the pair exactly as it was, 96.49%, and adding a `u32 flagWord = pSprite->flags_0xA8;` read after it is identical - so neither qty/creation order nor refs reach this one either, and the `+4` it costs is the mask constant moving out of $v1 rather than a moved register.
- id: a-masked-address-temp-breaks-a-giv-tie | keywords: regalloc, tie, giv, mask, temp, and, tag, local-alloc
  **ONE MIRRORED LEG OF A GIV_TIE MOVES IF THE MASKED VALUE (not the mask) IS COMPUTED INTO ITS OWN LOCAL BETWEEN THE LAST READ AND THE STORES - and the leg that is left is a local_alloc hard-register sort tie, provable with `-dl` in one compile** (round 417, world_map/main/misc13 func_80092FD8, 99.45 -> 99.58 at retail's exact 472 bytes / 118 insns, XenoWorldMapDiv preset). The tail is two tag read-modify-writes on a 0x28-byte prim record: `pPrim->tag = (pPrim->tag & 0xFF000000) | (ctx->pOt[0]->tag & 0xFFFFFF);` then `ctx->pOt[0]->tag = (ctx->pOt[0]->tag & 0xFF000000) | ((u32)pPrim & 0xFFFFFF);`. At 99.45 the base, element-pointer, index and tag word were already retail's and only the three materialised values were rotated (`0xFFFFFF`/`0xFF000000`/`&g_WorldMapRenderContext` in $a1/$a2/$a3 vs a rotation). Adding `primAddr = (u32)pPrim & 0xFFFFFF;` on its own line between them - found by the permuter in 1314 iterations - moved everything into retail's registers except a $a1/$a3 mirror, because the temp's single use lets cse keep one address pseudo instead of re-forming it in the second statement. Measured and NOT moving the rest (all 472 bytes / 118 insns): OR operands commuted 97.67, the two masks as `#define`s 99.58 (no byte change), the OT word in its own `u32` local 99.58, a `WorldMapPrim *pOt` local for `ctx->pOt[0]` 99.58 (gcc still reloads it through ctx after the store, so retail's two `lw 0x70(aN)` remain), the temp assigned above the tag read 99.58, cdk 90.97, -G0 99.58. The residual is a tie: in BOTH bodies the tail block uses $a1 four times, $a3 four times and $a2 three times, and the three pseudos are block-local (`-dl` prints `in block 10`), i.e. local_alloc's own hard-register hand-out with $a1/$a3 equal - same class as a-global-allocno-priority-is, so park after one `-dl` look. Second 3-minute permuter run from the 99.58 body: base 50 best 50, none.
- id: a-one-arm-local-spanning-a-call | keywords: regalloc, callee-saved, shared, local, call, live-range, frame
  **ONE LOCAL SHARED BY MANY ARMS AND LIVE ACROSS A CALL IN ANY ONE OF THEM COSTS A CALLEE-SAVED REGISTER FOR THE WHOLE FUNCTION, WHICH SHIFTS EVERY REGISTER AND THE FRAME - give the one call-spanning arm its own local** (round 416, slus_006.64/system/temp2 func_800248D4, 67.82 -> 69.15 in one edit, and the whole prologue then matched retail: frame 0x28, pSpriteData=s1, code=s2, frames=s3, pArgs=s0. The interpreter's scratch `count` is written in six arms and only the 0x82 arm (`stepY = p->step.y; if (cb) cb(p); SpritePlayAnimation(p, id); p->step.y = stepY;`) keeps it live across calls, so the single pseudo got s1 and pSpriteData/code/frames each moved up one. Declaring `s32 stepY;` for just that arm fixed all four registers and the frame with no byte change elsewhere. Companion lever measured in the same function: the arm dispatch must be a `goto` loop, not `while`/`for` - see g-switch-dispatch-makes-loop (61.25 -> 67.82 here, and it alone restored the 0x28 frame from 0x30).
- id: a-two-use-sum-does-not-tie | keywords: regalloc, tie, parameter, addu, cse, sum, in-place, slti
  **A SUM THAT MUST LIVE ACROSS A BRANCH IS A GLOBAL ALLOCNO, SO NEITHER AN IN-PLACE `+=` NOR A cse-MERGED SECOND USE PUTS IT IN THE DYING PARAMETER'S REGISTER - and the LOAD takes the register below instead** (round 410, battling/main/misc2 func_80073DE4, parked 98.61% at retail's exact 72 bytes / 18 insns, Default preset). Retail is `lh v1,0xB6(a0) / nop / addu a1,v1,a1 / slti v1,a1,0x1001 / bnez v1`: the sum is IN PLACE in the dying `arg1` and the load sits one register higher. Every body measured 18 insns / 72 bytes with the pair mirrored (`addu v1,v1,a1` / `slti v0,v1`): the two-statement load-then-`t = t + arg1` form 98.61 (also with `Actor *p0`, `(s16)p0->gaugePoints` and the division constants typed), an initialised declaration 98.06, the sum written twice so cse merges it into one two-use pseudo 98.06, `arg1 = hw + arg1` 97.78, and a `u32 amount = arg1` copy of the parameter (the parameter-copy-signedness lever) coalesces to no instruction and leaves the code identical. Two permuter runs from earlier rounds found nothing, and 2.6.0 / 2.7.2-cdk / -O3 / -G0 are all 97-98, so this is the local_alloc-vs-global-alloc class: park it. Companion typing fact from the same function: retail loads the SAME field offset 0xB6 with `lh` here and with `lhu` in the neighbouring BattleAccumulateGaugePoints, so a unit-wide field type is not the whole story - spell the signed read `(s16)p0->gaugePoints`.
Round 425 closed the mechanism on that same function with the `-da` `.lreg` dump plus a five-spelling cc1 probe. For the two-statement body the RTL makes the sum the SAME pseudo as the load - `(set (reg/v:SI 74) (plus:SI (reg/v:SI 74) (reg:SI 5 a1)))` with `REG_DEAD (reg:SI 5 a1)`, and `-dl` prints "Register 74 used 5 times across 7 insns" with no `in block N`, so 74 is LIVE INTO basic block 2 and is a global allocno handed `$v1` by REG_ALLOC_ORDER`; there is no separate load qty left for the allocator to leave in retail's `$v1` while the sum takes `$a1`. The probe (a standalone file compiled with the unit's cc_flags + `-O2 -G8`) of `arg1 += t`, `arg1 = arg1 + t`, `arg1 = t + arg1`, `s16` load + `s32` sum, and an initialised-declaration sum all emit `lh $2 / addu $3,... / slt $2` - load `$v0`, sum `$v1`, compare `$v0`, in every one. So a park note that says "the sum needs the dying parameter's register" on a 15-instruction `lh/addu/slti` body is closed: one dump plus one five-function probe settles it in two compiles instead of five diff runs. Round 438 confirmed both halves on the REAL body (18 insns / 72 bytes each): `t = arg1; t += (s16)p0->gaugePoints;` gives a sum pseudo distinct from the load and still lands the sum in `$v1` with `$a1` demoted to a source (`lh v0 / addu v1,a1,v0 / slti v0`, 97.78%), and the in-place `arg1 += (s16)p0->gaugePoints;` - the one C form that would give the sum the parameter's own home register - emits the identical 10 differences at 97.78, i.e. cc1 2.7.2 abandons a parameter's incoming register as soon as this block sets it. Do not measure this class again.
- id: a-preheader-copy-duplicating-a-parameter | keywords: regalloc, preheader, move, copy, parameter, callee-saved, frame, duplicate
  **A PREHEADER `move $fp,$tN` THAT DUPLICATES A VALUE RETAIL STILL HOLDS IN ANOTHER REGISTER MEANS THE SOURCE HAD A SECOND LOCAL ASSIGNED FROM IT - declaring that local restores the copy, the frame and the whole prologue in one edit** (round 406, slus_006.64/system/temp2_r1 func_800278F8, 72.41 -> 73.58, and the first 30 diff lines went from a 0x48 frame with `top` in `$s5` and every stack offset 8 low to retail's exact 0x50 frame with the same saved set). Retail keeps the parameter `top` in `$t3` (`subu v0,t3,t2` for the two upper vertices' y) AND in `$fp` (`sh fp,4(s0)` / `sh fp,0xC(s0)` for the two lower ones), and cc1 makes one register per pseudo, so the two must be two source values. Declaring `s32 yTop;` with `yTop = top;` after the early return and using it for just those two stores produced `move s8,t3` in the preheader; without it the allocator had no reason to duplicate and promoted `top` itself into a callee-saved register, which moved the frame and every stack slot. Reach for this whenever a diff's very first lines are a frame size plus a saved-set shift: count how many registers hold the same value in retail and declare the extra local.
- id: a-preheader-copy-of-a-slot-offset | keywords: regalloc, preheader, copy, callee-saved, frame, loop, base, move
  **A LOOP THAT REACHES ITS BUFFER AS `ptr + offset` WITH THE OFFSET IN ITS OWN `s32` LOCAL **alongside** THE INCREMENTING ONE RESTORES A MISSING CALLEE-SAVED COPY, THE FRAME AND THE WHOLE REGISTER BLOCK** (round 419, menu/main/misc4_260 func_801CF8D8, 91.32 -> 98.65 under XenoMenu260 at retail's exact 156 insns / 0x58 frame; the last two diff lines are the only residual). Retail keeps `base` in `$s3` AND a copy in `$s2` made by `move s2,s3` in the j-loop preheader, so y=$fp, scale=$s7, x1=$s6, count=$s5, x0=$s4, copy=$s2 and the `sw $s8` exists; with one variable the allocator has nothing to copy, base sits in `$s2` and all nine values drop a slot (153 insns, no s8). Declaring `s32 slotOffset;` and writing `slotOffset = base;` as a statement before the glyph loop while the cursor prim after the loop still uses `base` makes cc1 emit the preheader `move s2,s3`; the same assignment placed INSIDE the loop body is cse-folded back (155 insns), and `slotOffset = base` outside the `if` that guards the loop gives the copy but the wrong preheader order. Two companion measurements on this shape: the five loop-invariant `li`s must be assigned INSIDE the `if (state)` block or they come out in the prologue before the branch, and gcc emits them in source order, so retail's order is the statement order; the `u8 rendered[2]` zeroing is stored in reverse of the statement order in retail's prologue (`sb zero,0x21` before `0x20`), so write the reversed statements.
- id: a-himode-sum-of-two-byte-loads | keywords: himode, s16, tie, in-place, addu, lbu, clamp, allocno
  **A `s16` LOCAL SUMMING TWO ZERO-EXTENDED BYTE LOADS KEEPS RETAIL'S LOAD REGISTERS BUT CAN NEVER TIE IN PLACE, AND THE SImode SPELLINGS TIE BUT SHIFT BOTH LOADS - the two halves are one local-alloc decision and no type, cast, `+=`, volatile or block moves them together** (round 405, battle/main/misc19 func_8009A258, parked 99.68% at retail's exact 124 bytes / 31 insns). Retail is `lbu v0,20(v1) / lbu v1,91(a2) / addu v0,v0,v1 / move v1,v0 / andi v0,v1,0xff / sltiu v0,v0,0x65`: the HImode sum is IN PLACE in the dying p-byte register and the `u8` copy takes the freed q-byte register. With `short sum; u8 value; sum = p[0x14]; sum = sum + q[0x5B]; value = sum;` the loads and the copy are retail's and the add goes to a fresh `a0` (addu a0,v0,v1 / move v1,a0) - an HImode add expands through an SImode temp and a 3-operand `addu` has no copy suggestion from a dying input. The SImode family (s32/u16 sum, `volatile` q read at 99.19, `addu a1,v0,a1`) does tie but puts the loads in a1/v0, 99.03%; u8 byte locals and `volatile` p read 98.39; s16 via an s32 temp 98.39. At 99.68 and unchanged: `+=`, `(short)`/`(s32)` casts on the summands, a `short b` summand, a brace block, `register short`, declaration initialisers, q-first and commuted sums. Dropping the `value` copy (plain `if (value > 0x64) value = 0x64;`) costs the copy AND the bnez nop - 120 bytes, 95.97 - so the odd-looking `if (sum || sum) { value = 0x64; } else { value = 0x64; }` diamond is load-bearing for the size. Presets: 99.68 (this body) / 99.03 (s32) under 2.6.0, 95.97 under cdk; fifth permuter run PERMUTER_NONE base=10 best=10. Park on this shape instead of re-typing the sum. Round 418 closed the mechanism with the `-da` RTL dump (pseudo numbers are the dump's): `(reg/v:HI 87) = zero_extend:HI (mem p+0x14)` gets `$2`, `(reg:HI 104) = zero_extend:HI (mem q+0x5B)` gets `$3`, `(reg:SI 105) = plus:SI (subreg 87) (subreg 104)` gets `$4`, `(reg/v:QI 88) = subreg:QI (105)` gets `$3` - i.e. the RTL is already retail's but 105 cannot share 87's `$2`. The copy insn only EXISTS because the sum has a second use: with the plain clamp, combine folds `(set (subreg:SI (reg/v:QI 88)) (plus:SI ...))` and both the move and 4 bytes go (120 / 95.97). That second use is in the NEXT block, which makes 87/105 global allocnos, and global_alloc's order (88 first, then 105) hands 105 `$4` because `$2` is held by 87, whose live range overlaps 105's def - a 3-operand SI add whose output is a fresh allocno never shares the dying input's register, and local_alloc only ties when the two are in one block (that is why the 120-byte body is in place on the p load). A dead second use in the same block would give both, but it is deleted before combine: a `sum = 0;` after the copy re-measures 120 / 95.97. Sixth permuter run (round 418): base=10 best=10, 1429 iterations.
- id: a-post-branch-mask-of-a-cross-block-value | keywords: regalloc, in-place, mask, andi, li, compare, block-local, global-allocno, dl
  **AN `andi` + `li` + `bne` PAIR WHOSE ONLY RESIDUAL IS THE MIRRORED `$v0`/`$v1` CANNOT BE FLIPPED WHEN THE MASKED VALUE IS A GLOBAL ALLOCNO: the same instruction pair comes out RETAIL'S way in a block whose input is block-local and mirrored in one whose input crosses a branch** (round 421, slus_006.64/system/temp1 func_80023538, parked 99.89% at retail's exact 716 bytes / 178 insns, XenoGraphics). Two blocks of that function hold the identical qty pair: `field_0x3C_0 & 3 == 1` (block 18) and the tail `flags_0xA8 & 1 == 1` (block 23), both `2 refs / 3 insns` for the AND result against `2 refs / 4 insns` for the constant. `-dl` dispositions: block 18 gives the AND result `$2` and the constant `$3` (retail), block 23 gives the constant `$2` and the AND result `$3` (mirrored) - because block 18's input is a load in the SAME block that dies at the AND (local_alloc can tie the output to it, so `andi v0,v0,0x3` is in place), while block 23's input is the flags word live across the preceding `beqz` (a global allocno holding `$2`), which keeps `$2` occupied AT the AND even though it dies there; the freed `$2` then goes to the `li` constant. Two spellings measured dead on it: `flags &= 1;` on a `u32 flags` local assigned from the field (a compound assignment keeps ONE pseudo but `andsi3` is not a tied alternative, so the output is still a fresh allocno) 99.89, and the same test folded into the `&&` 99.89. So on this shape, read which block holds the input: block-local -> the tie is reachable by source, cross-block -> park it.

- id: a-split-constant-in-a-join-block-is-a-reload-pick | keywords: regalloc, mask, constant, join, lui, ori, reload, tie
  **AN UNLOADABLE MASK CONSTANT MATERIALISED IN A JOIN BLOCK (`lui`+`ori`, the `lui` reorg-sunk into the switch arms' delay slots) PICKS ITS REGISTER BY WHICH REGISTERS ARE OCCUPIED AT THAT POINT, AND EVERY OTHER REGISTER IN THE TAIL FOLLOWS FROM IT - so a park note that blames the stateFlags/flags pair is blaming the cascade, not the decision, and naming the constant in a local does not move it** (round 424, battling/main/misc4 func_8007920C, still parked 98.96% at retail's EXACT 1196 bytes / 299 insns, Default preset; instructions 1..224 identical, only the tail's register names differ). Retail gives `0xFFFEFFFF` `$a0`, `p->state` `$a1`, the flags word `$v0`, the stateFlags read and RMW result `$v1` - and because the mask is in `$a0` retail can emit the second constant's `li v1,-13` BEFORE `sw a0,208`, while this body's mask occupies `$v1`, so the store has to come first and `p->state` drops to `$a0`. Measured dead on it: `u32 mask; mask = 0xFFFEFFFF;` above the statement (exactly 98.96, 34 differing lines - the local coalesces into the constant, the same way a `u32 flags` copy local does), and the earlier `u32 flags` copy local + 1832-iteration permuter run (base 175 best 175). Same family as a-post-branch-mask-of-a-cross-block-value: the fix would be to free the register the mask wants before the join, which no spelling of these statements reaches.

- id: a-chain-and-a-scratch-need-two | keywords: regalloc, in-place, coalesce, scratch, read-modify-write, chain, tie, two-registers
  **WHEN RETAIL DOES A FLAG-WORD READ-MODIFY-WRITE IN ONE FRESH REGISTER WHILE A SCRATCH VALUE LOADED EARLIER LIVES IN ANOTHER, THE TWO HALVES ARE MUTUALLY EXCLUSIVE FROM SOURCE: pinning the scratch's early load means the chain reuses that same pseudo, and a chain that does not touch it lets cc1 sink the scratch's load and re-allocate the whole entry block** (round 404, battling/main/misc4 func_80079DF0, parked 99.96% at retail's EXACT 1068 bytes / 267 insns, Default preset). Retail's chain is `lw v1,0xD0(s1) / and v1,v1,a1 / or v1,v1,v0 / sw v1,0xD0(s1)` - one fresh pseudo, load and both updates in place - while `g_BattleFrameRateLevel`, read 20 instructions earlier, sits in `a2` and is stored to the countdown late. Measured, all 1068 bytes / 267 insns unless noted: the one-expression chain assigned through the reuse-bearing scratch local (`value = (p1->flags & ~0x40) | (...); p1->flags = value;`) gets the load and the `and` in `v1` but the `or` in `a2`, because the `or`'s destination is that local's allocno - 99.96, and `p1->flags = (value = ...)` and a `u32` local are identical; an IN-PLACE compound chain on the same local (`value = p1->flags; value &= ~0x40; value |= ...; p1->flags = value;`) collapses to ONE pseudo so all four instructions are `a2` - 99.89, and no spelling can give it `a2` for the byte and `v1` for the chain, which is what retail does; giving the chain its OWN local in place does put the chain in `v1`/`v1`/`v1` (retail's) but the scratch's live range shrinks, sched1 sinks its `lbu` to the countdown store and the state-slot block is re-allocated (`move a0,s1` late, pState stores in `v0`) - 268 insns, 1072 bytes, 96.31%, and the plain one-expression form, a `u8` scratch and the volatile read of round 372 are all the same 96.31; `value = p1->flags; p1->flags = (value & ~0x40) | (...);` is the complementary 99.96 (the `or` in `v1`, the load and `and` in `a2`). A 3-minute permuter run from the 99.96 body: base 10 best 10, 1311 iterations, NONE (an earlier 4-minute run from the same body: 1975, none), and try_flags gives 99.96 under gcc-2.7.2-cdk-psx and 99.96 under gcc-2.6.0-psx, so park this class instead of moving the unit's preset. Round 417 re-measured this function and closed two more spellings: PER-COMBATANT chain locals (`flags1 = p1->flags; flags1 &= ~0x40; flags1 |= ...;` and `flags2` for p2) reproduce retail's chain EXACTLY for both words (`lw v1,0xD0 / and v1,v1,a1 / or v1,v1,v0 / sw v1`, the `~0x40` in `a1`) - so the chain half is solved, and the whole residual is the frame-rate pseudo's register - but it is still 96.31% / 268 insns because the load then sinks; and the countdown store moved after the chains, `u8 value` and a `(u8)` cast at the store all leave that identical. It is a SCHEDULING artifact, not a statement-order one: compiling this unit's own `.i` with the unit's cc1 plus `-fno-schedule-insns` (a MIPS cc1 default at -O2 in 2.7.2, NOT a preset flag) puts the `lbu` back at the top of the block but shifts the slot-index/frame-rate/arg-copy registers all down one, so probe that flag before spending diff runs on a sunk load in this unit.
- id: a-boolean-local-typed-u-char | keywords: regalloc, boolean, u-char, sltiu, mask, move, tie
  **A BOOLEAN LOCAL THE BODY USES AS A SCALE TAKES RETAIL'S `sltiu`+`move` PAIR ONLY WHEN IT IS `u_char`, NOT `s32`** (round 399, menu/main/misc8 func_801D7CFC, 93.65 -> 97.08 under gcc-2.6.0-psx at retail's exact 596 bytes / 150 insns). Retail opens `andi a2,a2,0xff / sltiu a2,a2,1 / sw s4,48(sp) / move s4,a2` - the comparison lands in the argument register and is copied into the callee-saved one after its save; with `s32 selected = (arg2 == 0);` cc1 folds the mask into the compare and writes `sltiu s4,a2,1` directly, one instruction short. `u_char selected = (arg2 == 0);` reproduces the pair and everything downstream (`sll s0,s4,0x1` for the scale), and the remaining residual in that function is only the position of one base load. Measure the declaration type of a 0/1 local before parking a prologue whose only difference is a missing `move`.
- id: a-inline-field-reads-beat-locals | keywords: regalloc, register, local, inline, field, swapped, load, order
  **WHERE TWO FIELD LOADS FEED A DIFFERENCE AND BOTH VALUES ARE ALSO READ IN THE BRANCHY ARMS, DROPPING THE LOCALS AND READING THE FIELDS INLINE LETS cse PICK RETAIL'S REGISTERS AND LOAD ORDER - the locals cost both** (round 397, world_map/main/misc8 func_80085CDC 97.70 -> 99.18, 636 bytes / 158 insns = retail's exact size; residual is one store position, not a register). Retail holds `s16 ang` in `$a1` and `s32 cur` in `$a0` and loads them `lh`-then-`lw`; with locals declared as `s16 ang; s32 cur;` and assigned `ang = pActor->angle; cur = pActor->redStep;` gcc puts ang in `$a0`, cur in `$a1` and emits the `lw` before the `lh` - and swapping the two assignments, making ang `s32`, or the four declaration orders earlier rounds tried all emit that same mirrored body. Writing `d = pActor->angle - pActor->redStep;` with `pActor->redStep + 0x100` / `pActor->angle` in the two slew arms gets both registers and both loads right in one edit, because the pseudos are then created by cse in the order retail's allocator wanted rather than by two named locals. One `do { } while (0)` barrier or a field read-back as a call argument moved nothing (98.93 / identical), and a 3-minute permuter run found nothing (1433 iterations, base 130), so try this spelling before the permuter on any two-local field pair whose only residual is a swap.
- id: a-coalesce-loop-counter-is | keywords: regalloc, counter, iv, memory, loop, frame, permuter
  **AN OUTPUT-COALESCING COUNTER THAT INDEXES THE STORES CANNOT BE MADE MEMORY-RESIDENT FROM SOURCE: `loop.c` turns it into a pointer-iv chain and global-alloc then has a register for it, so retail's per-iteration `sw`/`lw` of the counter is an allocation this C does not express** (round 396, shop_menu/main/misc ShopMenuSellMenu, parked 98.61% at 2024 bytes against retail's 2032 - 2 instructions, the frame and every stack slot identical). Retail at 0xC05C: `addiu t4,t4,1 / sw t4,0x90($fp) / lbu v0,0(a2) / lw t4,0x90($fp)` while the three byte stores still run through the pointer ivs `t0`/`t1`/`t2`, and there is NO store of the initial 0 (the preheader is `addu t4,zero,zero`), so the variable is written by the loop yet its pre-loop value needs no store - which rules out both an ordinary MEM pseudo and a plain register pseudo. Every spelling that makes `itemCount` the counter moves the frame by 8 (itemCount takes `$s7`, pItemSellQtys is spilled): plain 95.95 at 2040 bytes, declaration initialiser 94.65, `u_int` 95.84, `+= 1` 95.95, two counters (`itemType++` and `itemCount++`, which `loop.c` merges back into one giv) 95.95; forcing MEM residency loses the pointer ivs instead (`int itemCount[1]` 90.79, a `*(volatile int *)&itemCount = itemCount + 1` store 91.27, both reloading the counter for each of the three stores), `volatile int itemCount` 90.49, itemType only (no itemCount local, the two while-loop reads using itemType) 95.16 at 2044. Preset ruled out on the same body with try_flags.sh: 2.7.2 -O2 -G8 97.01, 2.7.2-cdk 72.55. Park rather than permute (two earlier runs, 4 and 8 minutes, base 701 -> 666, found a declaration-order no-op and a semantics-changing write).
- id: a-coalesce-counter-is-a-copy | keywords: regalloc, counter, copy, coalesce, loop, store, twin, byte-count
  **AN OUTPUT-COALESCING LOOP 8 BYTES SHORT WITH A PER-ITERATION COUNTER STORE IS TWO PSEUDOS, NOT ONE MEMORY VARIABLE: move the twin's after-loop `itemCount = itemType;` into the block as `itemCount = ++itemType;` and retail's store/reload pair and byte count fall out** (round 419, shop_menu/main/misc ShopMenuSellMenu, 98.61 -> 99.73 at retail's exact 2032 bytes, which CLOSES a-coalesce-loop-counter-is and supersedes its "cannot be made memory-resident" verdict). That spelling reproduces retail's `addiu t4,t4,1 / sw t4,0x90(fp) / lbu v0,0(a2) / lw t4,0x90(fp)` verbatim with the three byte stores still on the pointer ivs `t0`/`t1`/`t2`, because the counter stays the itemType register pseudo (the twin's shape) and itemCount stays the memory-homed pseudo the twin also has - the store is a COPY between the two, which is why the pre-loop 0 needs no store and why the old entry's MEM-pseudo and register-pseudo candidates both failed. The pre-loop `itemType = 0;` stays; only the twin's after-loop assignment moves. What survives is a caller-saved rotation inside the loop: retail keeps the counter in `$t4` and i in `$a3`, but the itemType pseudo cannot leave `$a3` (it lives from the first loop, where retail has it in `$a3` too), so every fresh-pseudo spelling - itemCount as the index with `itemCount++` - re-opens the allocator contest the old entry measured (95.95 / 2040, itemCount takes `$s7` and pItemSellQtys is spilled, plus an extra slot and a swapped s6/s7 save order). One 3-minute permuter run on the 99.73 body: base=125 best=125, none. Not a rodata blocker: the C switch's table lands at +0x28 of the unit's .rodata, exactly jtbl_801C5028's offset, so this rotation is the only thing between the body and check_bin. Round 436 re-measured the park at 99.73% / 2032 bytes, confirmed with `check_bin.sh shop_menu` = OK, and closed the cheap axes so nobody spends another lane on it: the counter statement's POSITION in the block is free and gcc always emits retail's `addiu / sw / lbu / lw` order (`itemCount = ++itemType;` first 98.20, as written 99.73, `itemType++; itemCount = itemType;` 99.73, `itemCount = itemType + 1; itemType = itemCount;` 99.73, all 2032 bytes); reordering the three pointer stores gives 98.97 (owned first) and 99.71 (qtys first); a bare `{ }` barrier around the sellable block is 99.73; and a second 3-minute permuter run (1045 iterations) is base=125 best=125 again. The residual is exactly the eight-register hand-out {a1,a2,a3,t0..t4}: retail i=`$a3` counter=`$t4` ivs=`$t0/$t1/$t2` pItemTypes=`$t3`, ours one position off with the counter in `$a3`, and every spelling that emits these 2032 bytes ranks the eight the same way.
- id: a-address-taken-pointer-can-never | keywords: regalloc, callee-saved, address-taken, launder, memory, rematerialise, frame
  **AN ADDRESS-TAKEN LOCAL IS A MEMORY VARIABLE TO cc1, so "launder the constant address through a pointer whose address is taken" can hold a base in the frame but can never reach a callee-saved register - that is the wall, not a spelling** (round 396, field/main/misc2 func_8007254C, parked 91.81% at retail's exact 408 bytes). Retail keeps `&g_CamInterpolation` in `$s0` across the `FieldMatrixSetIdentity` jal and reaches `g_CameraEye` etc. as `-0x104($s0)`; the plain pointer form scores 62.01% because a constant pseudo is rematerialised per use, and adding a second pointer taken from the first (`pCamRef = &pCam; *pCamRef`) does give the displacement form (91.81%) but costs a frame slot plus a reload after the call, because gcc never register-allocates an addressable local. `register u_char* pCam` (no launder) is 62.01% again. Screening the LAUNDER body under other presets, which the old note had not done: gcc-2.6.0 -O2 -G0 = 93.88 (the allocator there does keep it), cdk compile-fails, 2.6.0 -O1 = 62.95, -O3 compile-fails - and the unit cannot move to 2.6.0 anyway (five matched neighbours drop to 95.50-99.86). Companion measurement from the same round's other function (func_80072D74): where cse DOES hold a twice-used field address in a register, the STATEMENT POSITION of an unrelated pointer initialiser decides which register it takes and is worth 0.07% - moving `pShake = (s16*)&g_Scene.shakeActive;` from just after the first step to just before the step-square multiplies took 98.99 -> 99.06.
  Round 452 measured the one launder that lever `a-dead-parameter-carries` would suggest, and it does NOT transfer to a SYMBOL base: declaring `void func_8007254C(u_char* pCam)` and assigning `pCam = (u_char*)&g_CamInterpolation;`, and the same with a `mem_addr` parameter plus a `u_char*` local, both fold the parameter pseudo back to the constant exactly like a fresh local - 62.01% / 520 bytes / 130 insns, i.e. identical to the plain pointer. That lever's win was a LITERAL address (0x1F8000A0 as a const_int); a symbol_ref assigned to a parameter is constant-propagated. With -G0 the only remaining route to retail's `$s0` would be a base loaded from memory, which this unit cannot supply without new data.
- id: a-symbol-plus-a-big-immediate | keywords: regalloc, register, lui, addiu, li, addu, dg, base
  **A `global + 0x8000` ADDRESS NEEDS `lui/addiu` PLUS `li`/`addu` (an addiu cannot encode +0x8000), and the address pseudo - a `reg/v` USER variable - always takes the LOWER of the two free registers, so a retail body with the constant in `$v0` and the base in `$v1` is only reachable when the base is not a source variable** (round 396, battle/main/misc31 func_800BB9D4, parked 84.11% at retail's exact 228 bytes / 57 insns, whole body one register pair off). `-dg` (regenerate with `docker run --rm -v"$PWD":/w -w /w --platform=linux/amd64 ethos:latest` and the unit's own cc1 + `cc_flags` + `-dg` on `build/src/<unit>.c.i`; the dump lands as `<input>.greg`, and is TRUNCATED for long functions, so read the tail only) shows insn 78/79 `(set (reg/v:SI 2 v0) (lo_sum ...))` for the base and insn 82 `(set (reg:SI 3 v1) (const_int 32768))` for the constant, then local-alloc hands the dead base register to the first load - retail's `lw v1,0xC84(v1)`. Measured: base and the two loads as locals 84.11 (base $v0), both loads inline in the call 83.44 (base $v1 at last, but gcc's right-to-left argument expansion loads 0xC84 before 0xC54 and depth lands in the `jal` slot), primIndex local + depth inline 83.44, `base + 0x8000` as one expression 220 bytes (it folds into the `%lo` addend and the `li`/`addu` disappear - so an exact-size park proves the original spelled the offset as a runtime add). The swap is a local-alloc decision between one 4-ref `reg/v` and one 1-ref scratch; nothing in the source moves it, so park on it.
  Round 434 added two measurements on the pair of battle/main/misc31 parks this lever names. (1) func_800BB9D4 (84.11 / 228 bytes / 57 insns exactly): spelling the base as a BLOCK-SCOPE DECLARATION-WITH-INITIALISER (`u8 *pSlotWork = g_BattleStateWork; pSlotWork += 0x8000;`) compiles to a byte-identical body, so the `a-declaration-with-initialiser-in-the-loop` mechanism does not reach a base-vs-constant tie either; the residual there is one decision seen three ways (base `$v1`/constant `$v0` mirrored, the base add emitted above retail's two argument moves, and retail's two loads straddling the `sw s2`/`sw s1` stack stores), all of which follow from the register hand-out. (2) func_800BB350 (`flags_0xA8 = (flags_0xA8 & 0x3FFFFFFF) | (slot << 30)`, 98.10 / 496 bytes): the flipped operand order `(slot << 30) | (flags & 0x3FFFFFFF)` is 97.78 and shows the mask's pseudo moves with it (`lui a0` instead of retail's `lui v1`), so on this read-modify-write the word, the mask AND the shifted value are three pseudos competing for two registers and the source order that fixes one rotates another - the same class as the mask-vs-word entry above, so park it at the exact-size mirror. (3) Round 447 added the two spellings that could still have moved it and both are dead: the round-417 in-place chain on a NAMED local (`flags = pSprite->flags_0xA8; flags &= 0x3FFFFFFF; flags |= slot << 30; pSprite->flags_0xA8 = flags;`) compiles BYTE-IDENTICAL to the one-expression body (98.10 / 496, so the chain collapses onto the load's pseudo here and the lever is neutral on this class), and a `do { } while (0)` around ONLY the flags_0xA8 store splits the block for local-alloc and costs an instruction (87.65 / 500). The shape retail needs is the mask forced into a register BETWEEN the shift and the word load, which `andsi3` cannot do because its constant is forced after the memory operand is expanded - that is the arithmetic proof behind this park.
- id: a-single-use-constant-local-is-folded | keywords: regalloc, const, local, fold, copy-prop, pseudo, regno, dl
  **A SINGLE-USE LOCAL HOLDING A MASK CONSTANT IS FOLDED BACK INTO THE `and`, SO IT CANNOT GIVE THE CONSTANT AN EARLIER PSEUDO THAN THE LOAD IT FIGHTS - and the `-dl` regno order is what proves a flag-word mirror unreachable** (round 418, battling/main/misc3 func_80076884, parked 99.43% at retail's exact 1972 bytes / 491 insns, Default preset). Retail's `p->flags = (p->flags & ~0x20000) | (spin << 17)` has the mask in `$v1` and the word in `$a0`; this body gets `$a0`/`$v1`, i.e. the mirror, and `cc1 -dl` on `build/src/<unit>.c.i` shows why: `(insn 188 (set (reg:SI 115) (mem 208)))` is the word and `(insn 1157/1158 ... (reg:SI 117))` the mask, so the mask's pseudo is created as 117 AFTER the word's 115 (expand_binop forces a non-legit constant only once the mem operand is expanded) and local-alloc, which allocates in regno order, hands the first free register ($3) to the word and $4 to the mask. Two spellings that look like a fix and are not: `u32 clearSpin = ~0x20000;` (or `u32 newFlags = p->flags;`) declared inside the block and used once - copy propagation folds both back and the code is IDENTICAL, so a named const local proves nothing; and reusing the function's existing `flags` variable for the second read (`flags = p->flags; ...`) - one pseudo per variable per function, so BOTH live ranges take the same register ($4 here) and the score drops to 99.41 with 30 differing lines. Writing the constant first, `(~0x20000 & p->flags)`, is canonicalised. Park on the mirror; the same class as a-bitfield-store-is-the-same-rtl.
- id: a-bitfield-store-is-the-same-rtl | keywords: regalloc, bitfield, read-modify-write, mask, tie, word, li
  **A FIELD WRITE THROUGH A BITFIELD COMPILES TO THE SAME `lw/and/ori/sw` RTL as the hand-written mask, so "the original is probably a bitfield store" is never the fix for a $v0/$v1 tie on a flag word** (round 393, slus_006.64/system/temp1 func_80023804, parked 99.10% at retail's exact 312 bytes / 78 insns). A local `union { u32 flags; struct { u32 unk0:7; u32 scale:12; u32 unk13:13; } fields; }` view of SpriteData's flags_0xAC written as `->fields.scale = 0x100` emitted byte-identical instructions to `(flags & 0xFFF8007F) | ((0x100 & 0xFFF) << 7)` (mask in $v1, loaded word in $v0, the reverse of retail), and a 3-minute permuter run (1309 iterations, base 70 best 70) found nothing. The tie itself is worth one diagnostic: in retail the constant mask takes $v0 and the loaded word $v1, and the SAME flip recurs in the unit's func_8002435C (~3 mask $v0 / 0x3C word $v1, 99.19%), so when two functions of one unit flip the same way it is one allocator decision to leave parked, not N ties to chase. Round 408 re-measured func_8002435C's half of that pair and closed the spellings that could have moved it: the mask as a positive constant (`& 0xFFFFFFFC`), the mask in a `u32` local assigned as the body's first statement, and the C operand order reversed (`0xFFFFFFFC & word`) all give the identical mirrored body (99.19% / 456 bytes / 114 insns), because cc1 canonicalises the commute; the word's pseudo is created first by expand_binop and both pseudos have refs=1, i.e. allocator priority 0, so the allocno number decides and the constant always loses `$v0`. Presets on the same body: 2.7.2 94.97, cdk 99.19. On a `(word & mask) | k` flag update, park instead of trying operand orders. Round 409 closed the residue arithmetically: mips.md's `andsi3` takes its constant as 'd', so
