`force_reg` creates the mask's pseudo AFTER `expand_binop` created the loaded word's, and local-alloc's
priority `floor_log2(n_refs)*n_refs/live_length*size` is 6 for the word (3 refs over the 2 insns load..and,
in place) against 4 for the mask (3 refs over its 3 insns li/ori/and) - the word wins on live length in every
spelling, so the mask can never be given $v0. Retail's OWN 0x3C chain in the same function shows the rule
holding (`lw v0,60(t0)` word in $v0, the three big mask constants in $a2/$a1/$a0), which makes the 0xAC
statement the exception rather than evidence of a different source. Re-measured in round 409: the 12-bit
bitfield spelling is again byte-identical (99.10% / 312 bytes), gcc-2.7.2-psx scores this body 77.18 (so
XenoGraphics' cdk -O2 is right and the unit cannot move), and a second 3-minute permuter run (1001
iterations) is base 70 best 70.
- id: a-base-register-takes-earliest-death | keywords: regalloc, register, base, swapped, death, chain, store, tie
  **TWO IDENTICAL `|=` CHAINS ARE PAIRED TO `$a0`/`$a1` BY RTL DEATH ORDER, NOT BY EMISSION ORDER, so retail whose emitted chain order and register pair disagree was reordered by the post-reload scheduler and cannot be typed** (round 393, battling/main/misc7 func_8007F854, parked 99.17% at retail's exact 96 bytes/24 insns). Four bodies pin the rule - which chain's base pseudo DIES first in RTL (i.e. in source statement order) takes `$a0`, and its value takes `$v0`: plain `A[0] |= K; B[0] |= K;` 96.25 (`$a0`/`$v0` = first chain), the same statements reversed 97.50, `v = A[0] | K; A[0] = v; B[0] |= K;` 98.33, and `A[0] |= K; return B[0] |= K;` 97.92. The return form is the useful find on its own: a RETURNED value is forced into `$2`/`$v0` whatever the statement order, so when retail's LAST chain holds `$v0` and an earlier one holds `$v1` the original returned that chain's value (it reproduced retail's entire order - both `la`, both loads, both oris, both stores, the `lw ra` slot - with only the two BASE registers left swapped, so on a candidates list prefer that body over a permuter body one instruction away). When retail's register pair and its emitted chain order contradict each other, however, source order moves both together and only sched2 could decouple them: `try_flags.sh --sweep` at 97.5 (2.7.2 -O2/-O3 x -G4/-G8; 2.6.0 88.75, cdk 86.5) rules out the preset, and park rather than permute. Round 406 re-confirmed it from the other direction and closed the permuter: the two plain statements in retail's OWN emitted order (98800 then 970E4) are 96.25 with the bases mirrored (`$a0` = 98800), the two REVERSED are 95.42 with retail's exact register pair and only the chain emission order mirrored (the cleanest body there is, one scheduler decision from 100), and a fourth 3-minute permuter run from the 99.17 park (2022 iterations, base=20) found nothing - so the 99.17 park and the 97.92 return form bracket retail and no C reaches between them. Do not spend another permuter run here. Round 422 re-measured the park at 99.17 (residual is only the two STORES swapped around the `lw ra` fill, every address/load/ori already retail's) and measured two more shapes, both exactly 96.25: loading both words before storing either (`a = A[0]; b = B[0]; A[0] = a | K; B[0] = b | K;`) compiles byte-identically to the plain two statements, and a pointer local for the first chain with the second spelled directly (`p = A; p[0] |= K; B[0] |= K;`) likewise - so neither load-batching nor a one-sided pointer local moves the store order off the `$a0`/`$v0` pair, which confirms the bracket from two new directions.
- id: a-empty-do-while-block-nudges | keywords: regalloc, block, do-while, addu, operand, tie, permuter
- id: a-do-while-around-a-store-makes-an-sched2-barrier | keywords: do-while, block, sched2, store, load, hoist, nop, position
  **A `do { ... } while (0)` AROUND ONE STORE ENDS THE BASIC BLOCK, SO sched2 CANNOT HOIST THE NEXT STATEMENT'S GLOBAL LOAD ABOVE IT - that is how retail's `lw <base>` / `nop` / `sw` adjacency comes back, and it is worth exactly the load-delay `nop` of each store whose value comes from a function call** (round 432, field/scripts/virtual_machine func_800A3474, 97.79 -> 98.44 -> 99.08% at retail's exact 518 insns / 2072 bytes, Default preset). The body stores two `HeapAlloc` results into `g_FieldActors[i].pActorData` fields and immediately re-evaluates the same expression for the following `memcpy`'s source; plain statements give `lw a0,76(v0)` / [next statement's `lui`+`lw g_FieldActors`] / `sw v0,272(a0)`, i.e. the store sinks below the next evaluation, where retail keeps it adjacent and pays the nop. Wrapping ONLY the store (`do { pActorData->pTargetTrack = HeapAlloc(0xC, 0); } while (0);`) closed the site in both artwork branches, one per branch, no instruction cost. Measured and worse on the same body, so do not apply it everywhere: a do-while around the pScriptWorkWords store costs 2 instructions (98.84, 520) and around a plain `short` field store 97.90 (30 differing lines) - it is per-site, and the sites that want it are the ones whose RHS is a `jal` result.
  **AN EMPTY `do { } while (0)` AROUND ONE LOOP BODY MOVES A $v0/$v1 TIE AND COSTS NO INSTRUCTION** (round 392, menu/main/misc9 func_801DD5E8, 99.58 -> 99.72 at retail's exact 424 bytes / 106 insns; `permute.sh` found it in 1403 iterations, base=45 best=30, and its only edit was that block). Wrapping loop 2's single call in `do { ... } while (0)` moved that loop's `g_Menu`/panel-base pair from `$v1` into retail's `$v0` with the same instruction multiset; the same trick around a different statement in the same function changed nothing, so measure it per loop body, not per function. The tie it does NOT fix, measured in the same round with six spellings: for `base + i * 0x80` where the index is an already-cse'd register and the base is a global field loaded at the use site, cc1 2.6.0 emits the OFFSET first (`addu v0,s0,v1`) for every source order - base-first cast, the `sizeof(struct)` form, an index-first cast, a whole-entry pointer local (94.40%, 412 bytes), a base local assigned inside the `if` (99.48%), a `MenuString*` local inside the loop (96.64%, 416 bytes) - where retail has the base first. The preset is not the cause: `try_flags.sh` on the same body scores 91.32 under both gcc-2.7.2 and gcc-2.7.2-cdk against 99.72 under the unit's 2.6.0.
- id: a-global-allocno-priority-swap | keywords: regalloc, register, swapped, dg, allocno, live, loop
  **`-dg` NAMES WHICH ALLOCNO TAKES `$t0`, AND A VALUE REFERENCED ONLY OUTSIDE A LOOP ALWAYS COMES LAST** (round 392, battle/main/misc11 func_8007FBE0, parked 99.39% at retail's exact 264 bytes/66 insns: retail count=$t0, limit=$t1, color=$t2, the body gives color=$t0, count=$t2). Run cpp with `-DSKIP_ASM` and then the unit's own `tools/gcc-*-psx/cc1` with the unit's `cc_flags` plus `-O2 -G8 -dg`; the dump lands next to the input as `<input>.greg` and its `N regs to allocate: ...` line is the PROCESSING order (first long-lived allocno takes `$t0`), with `Register dispositions` giving `allocno in <hard reg>`. Here colour (def in the loop preheader, read inside the loop) was processed first and count - live across the whole loop but referenced only before and after it - last, and no C spelling moves that: colour as s16 (99.39, same swap), as u32/s32/u8 (97.88, +4 bytes), colour declared or assigned first (99.39 / 96.44), `*pSrc` with `pSrc++` (99.09, also moves the pSrc base into `$a1`), `color = 0xC` written inside the loop (96.82), a plain `0x12` literal or `0xC + 6` (94.39, GCSE hoists both constants into `$t0`), dropping the guard's debris local for `if (arg0 > 1)` (allocation changes, both colour values in `$t0`); two 4-minute permuter runs (base 245 -> 50 -> 40) and a third 3-minute run (base 60 best 60) found nothing. Retail's two different registers for the 0xC and the in-loop 0x12 (`$t2`/`$v1`) also prove the original was not one variable, since one pseudo can hold one register. Treat this as BLOCKED, not as a tie to hand-edit. Round 405 closed the loop on it with the `-dl` pass, which prints the three terms of the quotient per pseudo (`Register N used X times across Y insns; Z bytes`): count 4 refs / 49 insns / 1 byte = 0.16, limit 4 / 36 / 4 bytes = 0.89, colour 7 / 28 / 2 bytes = 1.00, whose descending order 78-79-76 IS the `-dg` processing order and the `$t0`/`$t1`/`$t2` hand-out. Since count's four refs are exactly the move, the masked compare, the branch-set and the final store, and its live range is entry-to-last-store, and it must be QI (retail's copy from `arg1` is a bare `move`, no zero-extend), no spelling can lift it over limit's 0.89 - so read `-dl` before spending a permuter run on a mirrored temp: `-dg` gives the order, `-dl` gives the numbers that prove whether the order is reachable. Measured the same round: `u8 count = arg1;` as an initialised declaration instead of an assignment is byte-identical at 99.39, a fourth 3-minute permuter run (1495 iterations, base 40 best 40) found nothing, and try_flags gives 99.39 under gcc-2.6.0-psx and 85.14 under gcc-2.7.2-cdk-psx.
- id: a-global-allocno-priority-is | keywords: regalloc, register, swapped, dg, allocno, priority, size, refs
  **THE `-dg` PROCESSING ORDER IS `floor_log2(n_refs) * n_refs / live_length * size`, SO ONE LOOK AT IT DECIDES WHETHER A SWAPPED CALLEE-SAVED PAIR IS REACHABLE AT ALL** (round 393, menu/main/misc10 func_801E23CC, parked 99.66% at retail's exact 1812 bytes / 453 insns, whole body instruction-identical except that retail keeps the `s32 slot` in `$s2` and the `u8 lastSlot` in `$s1` and ours mirrors them). gcc-2.6.0's `global.c` `allocno_compare` sorts by that quotient (`* 10000 * allocno_size`) and `find_reg` then hands the callee-saved registers out in ascending order along it, so a pair that mirrors is one pair whose two allocnos came out in the wrong order. Two things follow that save a lane: (1) `allocno_size` is the pseudo's MODE SIZE, so a QI (u8) variable carries a 1 where an SI one carries a 4, and the refs term is `floor_log2(refs) * refs` - a variable with 2x the refs of another is ~10x its priority and can never be made to lose the contest by spelling; (2) the divisor is `live_length`, so two values that both start in the loop preheader and span the loop are tied on that term and nothing about declaration order, statement order or declared width moves them. Here the dump (`;; 16 regs to allocate: ... 148 75 72 74 96 73 71 209 91 90`, `75 in 17  72 in 18  74 in 19  96 in 20 ...`, pseudo 75 = slot, 72 = lastSlot) shows the mirror is exactly `slot` processed one slot before `lastSlot`, which the formula makes unavoidable for this body: slot is SI and used 22 times, lastSlot QI and used 11 times, same live length. Retail's reversed order therefore came from a function this C cannot express, not from a missing spelling - stop after one `-dg` look instead of permuting. Measured the same round: `s32 lastSlot` with explicit `(u8)` casts at the loop test and at both calls keeps retail's two `andi` but measures 99.12% / 1804 bytes, and gcc-2.7.2-cdk scores 89.55 on the body (so the preset is not the way out either). Regenerate the dump with `tools/gcc-2.6.0-psx/cc1` and the unit's own `cc_flags` plus `-O2 -G8 -dg` on `build/src/<unit>.c.i`; it writes `<input>.greg`. Round 431 re-measured the same body and closed the remaining cheap axes: gcc-2.6.0 `-O3` 86.36, `-G0` does not compile, and a third permuter run (1304 iterations, 181 s) came back base=145 best=145, so the mirror really is fixed once the instruction stream is, and the reason is sharper than the quotient alone: after cse the ref count is a function of the emitted code, the mode size is pinned by retail's own `andi`, and the live length is the loop in both cases, so any body that emits these 453 instructions ranks the pair the same way and any body that ranks it the other way emits different instructions.
- id: a-in-place-update-of-a-load-temp | keywords: regalloc, in-place, temp, local, read-modify-write, mask, tie, group
  **A READ-MODIFY-WRITE WORD READS CLOSER TO RETAIL WHEN THE LOADED VALUE IS CARRIED THROUGH A NAMED TEMP AND UPDATED IN PLACE, because the extra assignment raises that pseudo's ref count and moves a whole register GROUP, not one pair** (round 402, world_map/main/misc13 func_80092FD8, 98.52 -> 99.45 at retail's exact 472 bytes / 118 insns). Retail's display-list tag write is `lw a0,0(v1) / and a0,a0,a2 / or a0,a0,v0 / sw a0,0(v1)` and the one-expression C spelling (`p->tag = (p->tag & 0xFF000000) | (ot->tag & 0xFFFFFF);`) mirrors base / element pointer / index AND the three mask-or-context values (42 differing lines). `tag = p->tag; tag = (tag & 0xFF000000) | (ot->tag & 0xFFFFFF); p->tag = tag;` puts the base, the element pointer, the index and the tag word all in retail's registers, leaving only the three rarest values in a 3-cycle (22 differing lines, 99.45). Measured, all at 472 bytes / 118 insns: storing straight from the temp expression 98.52 (so the second assignment back into the temp is the load-bearing part), a temp for the OT word instead 98.52, temps on BOTH statements 97.11, a temp on the second statement only 98.14, a `pCtx = g_WorldMapRenderContext;` local 98.52, all six declaration orders of the locals 98.52, and a 2-minute permuter run (923 iterations, base=65 best=65) found nothing. The survivor is a `-dl`-provable tie: the three competitors carry equal ref counts (3 each) over live ranges one instruction apart, so only pseudo numbering decides. Round 430 closed the mask's pseudo-creation order as a knob too: the 0xFFFFFF mask in a `u32 otMask` local used at BOTH mask sites becomes a callee-saved global allocno (480 bytes / 120 insns, 0.00%) and a third permuter run from the 99.58 body found nothing (1666 iterations, base 50), so the $a1/$a3 mirror is a hand-out local_alloc owns - park on it.
- id: a-refs-are-three-times | keywords: regalloc, register, swapped, lreg, refs, insns, local, mask
  **IN `-dl` A BLOCK-LOCAL QTY'S REF COUNT IS THREE TIMES THE NUMBER OF INSN THAT MENTION IT, so "give the loser one more use" means one more INSN, and a body whose two values each appear in 4 and 3 insns can never swap** (round 399, field/main/misc5 func_800A6E70, parked 99.56% at retail's exact 500 bytes / 125 insns, mirrored $v0/$v1 pair only). The dump reads `123 used 12 times across 7 insns` (the mask, 4 insns), `124 used 9 times across 6` (mask+0x10, 3 insns), `122 used 12 times across 22` (j*16), and the block hands out `$3` before `$2` (`72/77/122/123 in 3`, `82/90..100/124 in 2`), so the mask takes `$3` (pri `floor_log2(12)*12*4/7` = 20.6) and mask+0x10 `$2` (18). Retail's pairing (`j*16`/mask in `$2`) needs mask+0x10 to outrank the mask: pri 18 -> 24 requires a fourth insn mentioning it, and retail's stream has exactly one `andi`, one `addiu` from the mask, and two stores each. Useful in both directions: this is how to tell, from the dump alone, whether the flip needs ONE MORE INSN (spell a read-back, a store, a use) or is unreachable. Four permuter runs on that body (base=55 four times) agree. Round 421 added the comparator reading and three more measurements on the same body: local-alloc sorts qtys by `n_deaths / n_refs` and then by `live_length`, so with both dying once the 9-ref value (1/9) is processed before the 12-ref mask (1/12) and takes the first free register - a `$v0`/`$v1` mirror with a 4-insn-vs-3-insn split is therefore a proof, not a tie, and the fifth permuter run agrees (base=55 best=55, 1550 iterations); grouping the mask's two `sb` above the `+0x10` pair DOES hand the mask `$v0`, but the allocator re-orders the four `sb` with it (96.53%), so the store stream owns that register and a store-order flip is not a free knob; and a `u_char` store-view local of the mask (`u_char nUS = nU;`) costs one instruction and 4 bytes (98.96%, 504 bytes), so narrowing a value to shorten its ref count is not free either.
- id: a-a-near-miss-allocno-quotient | keywords: regalloc, allocno, priority, refs, live, margin, near-miss, dg
  **BEFORE CALLING A SWAPPED CALLEE-SAVED PAIR UNREACHABLE, READ THE MARGIN BETWEEN THE TWO QUOTIENTS: A 4% GAP IS TWO INSTRUCTIONS OF LIVE RANGE, NOT A WALL** (round 404, menu/main/misc3 func_801CBA4C, parked 96.29% at retail's exact 836 bytes / 209 insns with the whole body instruction-identical and only the text walker and the loop index mirrored between `$s0`/`$s1`). `-da` (`;; 21 regs to allocate: ... 263 76 75 267 ...`, `76 in 16` = `$s0`, `75 in 17` = `$s1`) plus `-dl` (`Register 76 used 28 times across 117 insns`; `Register 75 used 11 times across 36 insns`) give 76 = the shared loop index (4*28*10000/(117*4) = 2393) and 75 = the string walker (3*11*10000/(36*4) = 2291), so the walker missing `$s0` needs live_length <= 34 (two instructions less) or 12 refs at live_length <= 37 - a margin a spelling COULD close, unlike the 10x gaps the two levers above describe. Two things make it not closable here and worth knowing: the walker's live range starts at the `lui/addiu` that `loop.c` hoists to the HEAD of the preheader, so moving the initialiser anywhere in the source's preheader measures identically (96.29 before and after), and giving the earlier loops their own index drops that pseudo to a block-local caller-saved register (95.93) because retail reuses `$s1` for both loops, i.e. one variable live across the call. So: one `-da` look separates "2 instructions away" (keep trying spellings that change refs or live range) from "10x away" (park at once), and the dump must be taken in ONE container run - `-da` writes `<input>.lreg`/`.greg` next to the input, and `docker run --rm` throws them away, so compile and print in the same `bash -c` (copy `build/src/<unit>.c.i` into `/tmp/w` first).
- id: a-copy-suggestion-drags-its-source | keywords: regalloc, dg, dl, copy-suggestion, andi, in-place, quotient, live
  **AN IN-PLACE `andi` (a mask computed from an existing value) ALLOCATES BY COPY SUGGESTION FROM ITS SOURCE, SO THE MASK PICKS THE HARD REGISTER FIRST AND DRAGS THE SOURCE VALUE INTO IT - and when retail's stream pins both ends of that mask's live range, the 12% quotient gap to the value it must lose to is unclosable** (round 412, field/main/misc5 func_800A6E70, re-parked 99.56% at retail's exact 500 bytes / 125 insns, Default preset). Retail keeps `j*16` and its mask in `$v0` (`sll v0,s2,0x4 / sh v0,8(s0) / sh v0,32(s0) / andi v0,v0,0x3f / addiu v1,v0,0x10`) and the `+0x10` value in `$v1`; ours mirrors all eight lines. `-dl` on the body: 77 = the loop index (12 refs / 5 insns -> 28.8), 78 = the mask (12 / 7 -> 20.6), 79 = the `+0x10` value (9 / 6 -> 18.0), 122 = `j*16` (12 / 22 -> 6.5), dispositions `78 in 3 / 122 in 3 / 79 in 2` - the mask is the top of the queue after the index, takes `$3`, and the source value 122 follows it into `$3` (they share by construction); the 18.0 pseudo then falls to `$2`. Retail needs 79 above 78, i.e. mask live_length >= 9 or 79 at 11 refs, and BOTH are pinned: the `andi` sits right after the two `sh` stores and its last use is the `u2` store, so no source position moves the range, and 79's fourth mention would be a 126th instruction retail does not have. Two further facts from this body: a same-width `u_short`/`u_char` retype of the mask moves the quotient the WRONG way (94.91 / 94.9 - QI size 1), and the preset is not the answer (2.7.2-cdk 99.52, 2.6.0 99.56, -O3 compile-fails). Screen a mirrored `$v0`/`$v1` pair whose lower quotient is a mask before permuting: a copy-suggested qty is not free to lose. Round 438 re-read the same body's dumps and settled the comparator: the dispositions are `78 in 3 / 122 in 3 / 123 in 2`, i.e. the `+0x10` value holds `$2`, the first-handed caller-saved register, so it was processed BEFORE the mask - which means the sort key is `n_deaths / n_refs` (1/9 > 1/12) and NOT `refs / live_length` (1.5 < 1.71, the round-412 reading, which predicts the mirror). The four terms a flip could come from are therefore nU1 at 13 refs (5 insns), the mask at 2 deaths, the mask at live_length 5, or nU1 at live_length 8, and retail's own stream pins all four (one `andi`, one `addiu` off it, two stores each per value). Measured dead the same round on that body: an `u_int` block-scope copy of the mask+0x10 (copy-propagated away, 99.56% identical), a full `try_flags.sh --sweep` (2.7.2 / 2.6.0 / 2.7.2-cdk x -O1/-O2/-O3 x -G4/-G8 x -mcpu 3000/r8000: best 99.56 under 2.7.2 and 2.6.0 at -O2, cdk 99.52, -O1 75.83, -O3 does not compile), and a sixth permuter run (1423 iterations, base=55 best=55) - six runs, base=55 every time, so on this class stop at the dump.

- id: a-lreg-dump-gives-the | keywords: regalloc, register, swapped, dg, lreg, refs, live, priority
  **THE `-dl`/`-da` LREG DUMP PRINTS THE THREE TERMS OF THE PRIORITY QUOTIENT ITSELF (`Register N used X times across Y insns; ... Z bytes`), so a three-way callee-saved rotation can be PROVEN unreachable in one 10-second compile instead of a session of spellings** (round 396, menu/main/misc1 func_801C6400, parked 98.62% at retail's exact 500 bytes / 125 insns, whole body instruction-identical except that retail keeps pBuffer=`$s0`, i=`$s1`, lineCount=`$s2` and ours rotates them). `-dg` alone gives the order and no numbers, and the numbers are what decide it: i is used 38 times across 70 insns (SI, size 4) -> `floor_log2(38)*38/70*4` = 10.86, lineCount 8 times across 34 (2 bytes) -> 1.41, pBuffer 6 times across 42 -> 1.14, which is exactly the processed order `11 regs to allocate: 119 72 73 142 146 74 71 ...` and hence the `$s0/$s1/$s2` hand-out. Retail's order needs a 6-reference pseudo to outrank a 38-reference one, i.e. 10x, so no C spelling of that body reaches it - the same verdict as `a-global-allocno-priority-is` at a far cheaper price. One extra spelling measured on the way: naming the scanned byte (`u8 c = pBuffer[i];` in place of the two `pBuffer[i]` tests, which cse already merges) leaves the machine code and the whole allocation unchanged at 98.62%, so the "a named temp adds an allocno and reshuffles the sort" hope is dead for this class. Dump recipe: cpp with `-DSKIP_ASM`, then the unit's own `tools/gcc-*-psx/cc1` with its `cc_flags` plus `-O2 -G8 -da`; `m.i.lreg` holds the per-pseudo lines and `m.i.greg` the order and dispositions (allocno numbers there ARE pseudo numbers). Round 419 closed the last cheap axis on that same body: ALL SIX declaration orders of the three candidates (pBuffer/i/lineCount permuted among their slots, the other four declarations fixed) measure 98.62% / 500 bytes unchanged, so the hand-out is not sensitive to pseudo numbering or declaration order - do not spend a lane re-ordering locals to move a callee-saved hand-out when the machine code is otherwise instruction-identical.

- id: a-the-conflict-line-settles | keywords: regalloc, conflict, caller-saved, rotation, da, dreg, proof, tie
  **FOR A CALLER-SAVED ROTATION ($a1/$a2/$a3) THE LEVER IS THE CONFLICT LINE, NOT THE PRIORITY QUOTIENT: `m.i.greg` prints `<N> conflicts: <hard regs>` and any register listed there is impossible for that pseudo, so one 10-second `-da` compile can prove a three-register rotation unreachable instead of a session of spellings** (round 398, world_map/main/misc6 func_80080600, parked 99.69% at retail's exact 768 bytes / 192 insns, whole function byte-identical except that retail keeps nValue=`$a1`, `&D_8009BD42`=`$a3`, the reloaded scratchpad=`$a2` and ours rotates the three cyclically). The dump's `79 conflicts: 73 79 2 3 4 5 6 29 64 65 66` lists v0, v1, a0, a1 and a2 (plus sp and hi/lo/accum from the `divmodsi4` the value comes out of), so `find_reg` has only `$a3` left and MUST put nValue there - the priority quotient is irrelevant here, and `79` was in fact second in `13 regs to allocate: 80 79 ...`. Retail's RTL therefore differs structurally in a way no shape of that tail reaches, while the RTL we have is retail's down to `mfhi` going straight into the value, `sra a0,13` for the second shift and `li v0,1` in between. Two shapes measured on the way: the one-statement `nValue = rand() % (unk7C >> 12) - (unk7C >> 13);` splits the value into two pseudos (`mfhi v1`, `subu a3,v1,a0`) and stays 99.69 with an extra `move`, so the two-statement split is the shape retail has; and the whole preset screen (`-G0`, gcc 2.6.0, cdk) plus two permuter runs (2011 and 2388 iterations, base 60) had already come back empty, which is the expected outcome once the conflict line rules the spelling out.
- id: a-in-place-parameter-shift | keywords: regalloc, swapped, shift, parameter, switch, arm, merge, sll
  **A SHIFTED ARGUMENT THAT RETAIL SHIFTS IN PLACE (`sll a2,a2,0x8` as the arm's first instruction) IS A COMPOUND ASSIGNMENT TO THE PARAMETER, NOT `(value << 8)` INSIDE THE STORE: the inline form keeps `value` live into the shared tail, makes a fresh temp, and mirrors the whole register pair** (round 398, field/main/misc11 func_800924D4, 63.71 -> 100.00 at retail's exact 204 bytes / 51 insns, plain Default preset). Retail's four-arm byte setter shifts `$a2` at the head of each arm, holds the mask constant in `$a0` and `index * 4` in `$v1`; `g_FieldWalkmeshMaterialFlags[index] = (g_FieldWalkmeshMaterialFlags[index] & 0xFFFF00FF) | (value << 8);` scored 63.71 with the pair mirrored (mask `$v1`, base `$a0`), the shift emitted late (`sll v1,a2,0x8`) and the arms' tails cross-jumped into a different shared block (228 bytes). Writing `value <<= 8;` as the arm's FIRST statement and `| value` in the store reproduces retail instruction for instruction, after which the constants spell as `~(0xFF << 8)` with no byte change. So when retail shifts an argument into its new position before touching memory, try the in-place shift before blaming the allocator: it is what makes cross-jumping merge the arms the way retail's are merged.
- id: b-himode-temp-flips-the | keywords: regalloc, register, swapped, tie, clamp, himode, s16, allocno
  **A SHORT-LIVED CLAMP TEMP THAT MIRRORS A LONG-LIVED VALUE ACROSS `$v1`/`$a0` IS FLIPPED BY DROPPING THE TEMP TO HImode (`s16`), NOT BY SPLITTING OR REORDERING IT** (round 394, battling/main/misc11 func_80087698, 98.69 -> 100.00 at retail's exact 260 bytes / 65 insns; retail keeps the interpolated per-column value in `$v1` and the table byte it clamps against in `$a0`, mirrored in ours). `-dg` shows why the `s32` body cannot win: `;; 10 regs to allocate: 78 80 81 82 73 72 74 75 76 77` puts the clamp temp (78) ahead of the value (80), and 78 lists hard reg `2` ($v0) among its conflicts (the `slt` results live there), so `find_reg` hands it `$3` and the value `$4` - the priority quotient of the previous lever, with `allocno_size` the only term a spelling can move here (refs 6-vs-9 and the loop's live_length are fixed by the body). Declaring only the clamp temp `s16` flips the pair to retail's, in both clamp halves and with the pointer and loop values untouched. Measured in the same round: `s32` 98.69, `u16` 98.38 (the `D_800918B4[i] + 1` of 255+1 does not fit u16, so an `andi a0,v0,0xffff` appears), `u8`/QI 95.23 and also flips the pair but takes the pointer's `$a2` and the loop's `$t0`/`$t1` with it when the halves keep separate temps (95.92 with the array spelling), two separate `s32` temps for the two halves 95.23 (pair flips right, `p` displaced to `$t0`, `cur`/`step` to `$t1`/`$t2`), and an `s16` SWAP temp costs a `sll`/`sra` pair at 97.54 - so the narrow type must sit on the temp whose value is a table byte, and nowhere else.
- id: a-dead-parameter-carries | keywords: regalloc, register, cse, constant, parameter, scratchpad, copy
  **A DEAD PARAMETER REUSED AS AN INT SCRATCH is how retail's one register-held copy of a constant address is spelled** (round 389, world_map/misc5 func_8007CF18, 95.72 -> 100.00 at retail's exact 352 bytes). Six stores to a fixed address (0x1F8000A0) plus two `jal` arguments make cse hold the address in ONE callee-saved register ($s2) and shape five of the six stores as `lui $at / sh`, using the register for exactly one. Which store consumes the register is not reachable by spelling: the natural `SVECTOR *pTilePos = (SVECTOR *)0x1F8000A0` (all three stores, or one arm only), a `s32`/`u32` local holding the same constant, an `s32` local assigned the literal, `register s32`, a pointer local assigned at each of the three positions, and `volatile` on the first store all compiled to the SAME 348-byte/87-instruction body that uses the register twice (one instruction short of retail). Assigning the constant to the now-dead INDEX PARAMETER (`idx = 0x1F8000A0;` then `((SVECTOR *)idx)->vz = ...;`, found by `permute.sh` in 2439 iterations) gives retail's instruction for instruction, because a parameter pseudo is not constant-propagated into a memory address the way a fresh local is. It is load-bearing debris: comment it and report the function ROUGH.
- id: a-constant-cse-is-per-mode | keywords: regalloc, cse, constant, mode, li, u8, argument
  **cse's "register holds constant K" knowledge is per MODE, so a narrow-typed competitor is what lets a literal argument keep its own `li`** (round 392, battle/main/misc15 func_8008BED8, parked 99.42% at 1156 vs retail's 1160). Retail materialises `li s0,1` for the 7th stack argument of two calls while a `s32 pagePending = 1` already lives in `$s4`, and every `s32` spelling of the argument value (literal, `u8`/`u16` local, an `s32` local declared next to the calls, the variable itself) makes gcc store `$s4` instead - the source cannot separate two SImode constants of the same block. Declaring the COMPETING variable `u8` breaks the merge at once (cse keys the constant by its mode, so the QI const 1 is not the SI const 1) and reproduces retail's prologue exactly, including the `sw $s0,0x48($sp)` save position and the argument stores; here it then cost 2 instructions in the loop head (a QI variable needs `andi x,src,0xFF` for a `!= 0` test, and the derived `page = pagePending - 1` is recomputed), 1164 bytes, so the narrow type is not shippable - but the probe says the residual is the mode of the constant, not a spelling of the literal, and it explains why a byte store's constant gets its own register next to a word one (retail's s7/s6 pair of 2s). Reach for it when retail has two registers holding the same small constant and one of them feeds a byte access.
- id: a-dead-load-emitted-but | keywords: regalloc, register, callee-saved, cc1, frame, lw
  **A dead load is emitted but only reaches a callee-saved register when its value has a USE** (round 311, cc1_probe replica): cc1 keeps `pVramData = pTarget->pVramData;` with no reader, but puts it in `$v0` and the frame loses 8 bytes. So when retail has `lw $s3,0x24($s0)` and nothing in the function reads it, the original assigned that field to a local that a LATER CALL consumed - look for the argument that used it, not for the load.
- id: a-ternary-abs-over-a | keywords: regalloc, register, negu, abs, subu, ternary, copy, cse
  **A conditional-expression abs written over a NAMED difference keeps no copy (`negu r,r` in place, then reorg duplicates a following `slt` into the branch slot); spelling the same abs with the subtraction DUPLICATED inside the ternary makes gcc fold the negate into a reversed `subu` instead** (round 392, world_map/main/misc12 func_80090FB4, parked 98.03 at retail's exact 287 insns / 1148 bytes). Retail's two ramp abs are `subu v0,a0,v1 / bgez v0 / addu a1,v0,zero / negu a1,a1` - the copy into a SECOND register survives - while `nDelta = a0 - v1; nAbs = (nDelta < 0) ? -nDelta : nDelta;` coalesces nAbs into nDelta and emits `negu r,r`; writing `nAbs = (a0 - v1 < 0) ? -(a0 - v1) : (a0 - v1)` scores 95.73 because the negate becomes `subu $7,$4` (b-a). So: if retail shows `subu a,b` then `negu r,r`, the source negates a VARIABLE holding the difference, and a duplicated-subexpression abs is not that spelling. Neither spelling reproduced retail's surviving copy here; the copy is one allocator choice that cascades into every register in the arm (retail pad a0 / nTurn v1 / nAngle a0 + copy a2, ours v1 / a1 / a2 + copy a0), and cdk scores 93.66 on the same body.
  Confirmed one round later on the DUPLICATED form's home ground (round 415, battle/main/misc34 func_800BFE48, XenoBattleTailCdk0, cdk): retail's idle-slot distance test is `subu v0,a0,v1 / bgez v0 / slti v0,v0,9 / subu v0,v1,a0 / slti v0,v0,9 / beqz`, i.e. TWO separations off the same two operands, and only the duplicated arm gives it - `dx = a - b; if (dx < 0) dx = -dx;` emits `negu` there. Two companions measured on that body: the second operand of a `A >= 9 || B >= 9` chain gets its abs test laid out INVERTED (`bltz` plus an extra `j` back) where retail keeps `bgez` inline, so the `||` costs 6 bytes on that body, and the second test's operand still costs a `move a1,s1` copy of the sprite pointer (going through a `BattleFixed16Vector *pPosition` local, unmeasured direct member form: this unit's `SpriteData::position` is an `IVEC3` with no `xWhole`/`zWhole`).
- id: a-three-operand-or-never-ties | keywords: regalloc, register, or, in-place, op, tie, gte
  **A 3-operand `or` NEVER TIES TO ITS INPUT, so retail's in-place `or a0,a0,t7` came from an in-place C update, and using one re-shuffles the whole allocation** (round 391, battling/main/misc6 func_8007E020, parked 99.78 at retail's exact 556 bytes/138 insns). Retail's OT insert is `lw a0,0(v0) / and v1,a2,t2 / sw v1,0(v0) / or a0,a0,t7 / sw a0,0(v1)` and any `*pTag = link | K` body compiles the `or` into a FRESH register (`or v0,a0,t7`), because the MIPS `ior` pattern's output constraint is `=d`, not `0`, and local-alloc has no copy suggestion between an input and an output. Writing the update in place (`link |= K; *pTag = link;`) does give `or a0,a0,t7`, but it raises that pseudo's ref count and the loop's sum stops coalescing (`addu a1,v0,t6` instead of `addu v0,v0,t6`) and a callee-saved register appears: 93.51% at 568 bytes. Per-group link/pTag/pLink locals + the in-place OR reproduced retail's SECOND of three identical groups EXACTLY (98.63 overall) while the other two swapped the link/masked pair ($3/$4 instead of $4/$3), so the residual is a local-alloc priority contest between the loaded-link qty and the masked-address qty, decided per group. Naming `masked` (the `(u32)pQuad & 0xFFFFFF` value) makes a group exact but the name must not be shared between groups: shared, that pseudo lives across the groups and costs `sw s1,4(sp)` (94.48 / 92.43 at 568 bytes). Two 10-minute permuter runs (1990 and 4335 iterations) on the 99.78 body: base 30 best 30, no lead; a third run from the same body (round 406, 1206 iterations in 3 minutes) is base 30 best 30 as well, so stop at two. Round 406 put `-dg`/-da on that body and it says the same thing structurally: the loop's 17 allocnos are processed `77 78 79 158 74 ...` and 77 (the OR's result) takes $v0 before 78 (masked) -> $v1 and 79 (the loaded link) -> $a0, so retail's `or a0,a0,t7` has NO pseudo of its own to allocate - the in-place C update is the only shape that can produce it. The same idiom DOES tie in a matched neighbour (BattleDrawLineQuads, `or v1,v1,t9`) only because $2/$3 are the only free registers there, so a matching neighbour's shape does not transfer.
- id: a-memcpy-dst-sum-tie | keywords: regalloc, register, memcpy, movstr, addiu, in-place, tie
  **A MEMCPY DESTINATION OF `p + N` SHOWS UP AS AN IN-PLACE `addiu v0,v0,N` ONLY WHEN local-alloc wins a priority contest, and the `pDst = g; pDst += N;` copy spelling does not win it** (round 393, field/scripts/virtual_machine func_800A3F4C, parked 99.26% at retail's exact 2044 bytes / 511 insns). Retail advances a save cursor with `lw v0,g_WorldMapSaveCursor / addiu v0,v0,0x38 / move a3,v0 / ... / sw a3,g_WorldMapSaveCursor` at four chunk sites - the sum reuses the loaded pointer's `$2`, so no extra pseudo exists and `$3` stays free for a later `&global` (retail keeps that in `$s1`). The plain `pDst = g_WorldMapSaveCursor + 0x38; g_WorldMapSaveCursor = pDst; memcpy(pDst, src, 0x74);` body gives the load in `$2` and the sum in a fresh `$3`; `pDst = g; pDst += 0x38;` 99.10, moving the global store after the memcpy 93.23 at 2024 bytes, and reordered local declarations no change. The SAME shape ties in place elsewhere in the same function (the per-actor `+0x30` site) on both sides, so the tie is reachable - it is a per-site local-alloc priority contest, not a spelling. Pressure passive: gcc-2.6.0 98.88, gcc-2.7.2-cdk compile-failed, and a 3-minute permuter run (1273 iterations) on the 99.26 body: base 240 best 240.
- id: a-compound-assignment-on-the | keywords: memcpy, movstr, addiu, in-place, coalesce, cursor, tie
  **THE `pDst = cursor + N; cursor = pDst;` COPY SPELLING NEVER WINS THE MEMCPY-DESTINATION SUM TIE, BUT A COMPOUND ASSIGNMENT ON THE GLOBAL DOES: write `g_Cursor += N;` followed by `memcpy(g_Cursor, src, N)` and the sum reuses the loaded pointer's `$2` exactly as retail's `lw v0,cursor / addiu v0,v0,N / move a3,v0 / or v0,a3,a2 / sw a3,cursor` shows** (round 408, field/scripts/virtual_machine func_800A3F4C: 99.26 -> 99.82 on the four state-chunk sites alone, and 99.94 with the block head spelled the same way, at retail's exact 2044 bytes / 511 insns, ending 8 differing lines from 52). This supersedes the `a-memcpy-dst-sum-tie` conclusion for the cursor case: that park was right that no *copy* form (including `pDst = cursor; pDst += N;`, 99.10) reaches the in-place add, but the `+=` directly on the global is a different RTL (load, add and store share one pseudo) and it does. Same for the head, which needs all three statements: `cursor = block; block[0] = count; cursor += 4; memcpy(cursor, src, 0x38);` - writing `pDst = 4 + block; cursor = pDst;` instead mirrors the `$v0`/`$v1` pair for the base and the sum. The residual after this is one commutative `or` operand order (retail `or v0,a3,a2`, ours `or v0,a2,a3`, same registers and roles) and one callee-saved choice in the tail (`&g_Block` in `$s1` vs `$v1`); a 3-minute permuter run from the 99.94 body (1286 iterations) found nothing.
Round 421 re-measured the same body (99.94% / 2044 bytes / 511 insns unchanged, permuter again base=30 best=30 over 1375 iterations) and closed the `$s1` leg as a hand-out CLASS difference rather than a missing spelling: `$s1` is callee-saved and local-alloc gives a one-block value REG_ALLOC_ORDER's caller-saved registers first, so the retail `lui s1 / addiu s1 / subu a1,a1,s1` came from a pseudo the compiler classed differently. The only spelling that changes the class - one `pSaveBlock` assigned at the head and used again at the tail - measures 99.04% / 2044 bytes with the frame at 0x34, i.e. it buys `$s2` plus a save/restore instead of reusing the `$s1` the actor/gear index already saves, so sharing one variable is not the shape retail had. Byte-identical to the park and therefore dead: a tail-only `pSaveBlock` local, and a `do { } while (0)` wrapper around the tail. Park the two ties (this `$s1`, and the `or v0,a3,a2` operand order) instead of re-spelling.

- id: a-split-header-rect-out | keywords: regalloc, register, array, cse, hoist, pointer
  **Split a header RECT out of an array of RECTs when one register is doing double duty.** round 312: one `RECT rects[5]` indexed with `rects[count + 1]` makes gcc CSE the array base with a `LoadImage` argument, hoist it above the first call, spill it and rematerialise another argument (796 bytes, 81.88%); `RECT rect; RECT rects[4];` frees exactly the register that keeps the argument in `$s6`. Same family as the two-induction-pointer shape.
- id: a-reusing-existing-local-how | keywords: regalloc, register, callee-saved, gte
  **Reusing an EXISTING local is how you get a value into a callee-saved register** (round 321): a GTE operand like `mtc2 $s1,$8` only appears when the value's live range spans a call, so `rnd = pWork->depthBase + rand() % pWork->depthRange; gte_lddp(rnd)` reusing the `rnd` local puts it in `$s1`, where a separate `depth` local goes to caller-saved `$a0` (battling/misc16 97.73 -> 98.67). Same mechanism as the battle/misc26 packet-code trick: a value retail keeps in a register whose live range does not overlap another's can be expressed by reusing that local.
- id: a-two-similar-blocks-must | keywords: regalloc, register
  **Two similar blocks must not share their locals**: round 326's two threshold blocks see-sawed at 98.38 with one `roll`/`limit` pair and matched at 100.00 once the second block got its own `roll2`/`limit2` - allocation is coupled ACROSS the blocks, so measure the pair rather than one block.
- id: a-two-similar-blocks-must2 | keywords: regalloc, register, permuter, pointer
  **TWO SIMILAR BLOCKS MUST NOT SHARE A POINTER LOCAL - give each arm its own** (round 326 found it, round 335 closed a function with it): `pRow` and `pGearRow` as separate locals cost nothing (disjoint live ranges) and let each block accumulate in place, where one shared local takes a different register per basic block because gcc 2.x has no webs. battle/misc18 func_80098AF8 went 99.84 -> 100.00 on exactly that edit, after 24 declaration orders and a 2235-iteration permuter run had found nothing.
- id: a-shared-callee-saved-register | keywords: regalloc, register, callee-saved, jal
  **A SHARED CALLEE-SAVED REGISTER CAN BE THE BLOCK STRUCTURE, not an allocator tie** (round 335): if retail keeps a constant and a mask in callee-saved registers where your body folds them, check where the call sits. Retail duplicated `MenuSetFt4QuadVertices` in EACH arm and shared only the call AFTER the if/else; with both calls inside the arms gcc hoisted the constants across the first `jal` (92.91), duplicating the call took it to 95.15, and `originY = ...` before `u = ...` in each arm took it to 100.00 (menu/misc6 func_801D36E0).
- id: a-register-allocation-misses-usually | keywords: regalloc, register
  Register allocation misses usually come from local declaration order, split/merged temporaries, or a repeated field load turned into a local (or the reverse).
- id: a-op-redefinition-lever-round | keywords: regalloc, register, cse, move
  THE NO-OP REDEFINITION LEVER (round 155, world_map/misc3 func_800771D8, 99.67 -> 100): when the original does 'move v1,a2' then 'negu v1,v1' and yours folds it to 'negu v1,a2', reassigning the parameter to itself INSIDE the branch ('if (limit < 0) { arg2 = arg2; ... }') stops cse from folding the negate onto the parameter. It looks like debris and is load-bearing; leave a one-line comment.
- id: a-four-shapes-hand-editing | keywords: regalloc, register, cse, delay, loop, move, permuter
  FOUR SHAPES HAND-EDITING DOES NOT MOVE - if the only remaining difference is one of these, do not iterate by hand, run the permuter (RULES) and name the shape in your park note if it finds nothing: CSE_ARG (cse replaces a single-use sum pseudo at the argument position), BLOCK_ROTATION (retail has the loop increment at the TOP of the body block, in a beq delay slot), SCALAR_ADDR (retail keeps a scalar global's ADDRESS in a saved register and loads through it with two different indices; ours folds the address into each load), GIV_TIE (identical instruction multiset, only registers differ).
- id: a-bank-access-spelled-out-flips | keywords: regalloc, register, swapped, lui, addiu, struct, addu
  **A BASE-vs-OPERAND $v0/$v1 SWAP CLOSES WHEN THE BANK ACCESS IS SPELLED OUT AT BOTH SITES AND THE SHIFTED BYTE GOES FIRST** (round 389, battle/main/misc6 func_8007AC30 97.00 -> 100.00 and func_8007AC80 97.39 -> 100.00, both 20/23 instructions with an identical multiset). Retail held the `la` temp for the register-bank base in `$v0` and the operand temp in `$v1`; with `u16 *pWordRegs = g_BattleSlotWordRegs[index].wordRegs;` plus one read-modify-write, 2.6.0 put them the other way round for every declaration/operand order tried (and -O1/-O3/-G0 and cdk did not help). The matching source keeps NO pointer local - `g_BattleSlotWordRegs[index].wordRegs[pArgs[1]]`, spelled out at the load and at the store - and writes the two-byte immediate as `(pArgs[3] << 8) + pArgs[2]`: with the base in a local the tie follows the operand order instead and only reached 96.00. So for a whole-body $v0/$v1 swap on a `table[i].bank[k] op imm` shape, measure the FOUR combinations (pointer local vs spelled out) x (immediate order) rather than picking one. Confirmed again in round 398, battle/main/misc5 func_8007A968 (the dword-bank sibling, parked 53.44 -> instruction-identical): the `mem_addr regFileBase` local form mirrors the whole allocation at 94.38, and dropping it for the spelled-out `g_BattleSlotRegs[arg1].dwordRegs[p->operand1] = val;` with the immediate written `((p->operand3 << 8) + p->operand2) * 0x10` reproduces retail exactly (objdiff reads 99.38 only because the relocation folds g_BattleSlotRegs+0x10; check_bin OK).
  Round 403, battle/main/misc9_260 func_8007E6A0/func_8007E6F0 (both 100.00, 80 bytes, from 99.00 parks): the same body is 89.00 with the base declared as a 2-D `extern u32 g_BattleSlotRows[][16]` and the three accesses spelled `g_BattleSlotRows[index][pCmd->operandN]`, because the `la` lands in `$v1` and reorg then puts `lui/addiu` BEFORE the `sll 6`; declaring the 0x40-byte row as a struct (misc8.c's BattleSlotRow, `dwordRegs[4]/wordRegs[8]/byteRegs[0x10]/pad30`) and spelling `g_BattleSlotRows[index].dwordRegs[pCmd->operandN]` gives retail's `$a2` and the `sll` first. So when a `table[i].bank[k]` shape lands one register off with the address schedule reversed, the table's DECLARATION (2-D array vs struct row) is a lever, not just the local-vs-inline choice.
- id: a-literal-constant-outranks | keywords: regalloc, allocno, dg, constant, lui, tie, or, refs
  **ON A `value | LARGE_CONSTANT` TAIL THE CONSTANT'S ALLOCNO ALWAYS WINS `$v0`, because it has 2 refs over a 2-instruction live range and that quotient is the maximum** (round 395, slus_006.64/psyq/libgpu_b func_80045BCC, parked 98.53% at retail's exact 68 bytes / 17 insns, identical instruction multiset). Retail masks x into `$v0` and `lui`s 0xE5000000 into `$a0`; every body gives the mirror. `-dg` on the parked body (`;; 5 regs to allocate: 76 75 74 73 72`, `74 in 4  75 in 3  76 in 2`) puts the constant first and the masked-x allocno fourth, and swapping the masked x to SImode (4x the HImode `allocno_size`) or removing the load-bearing dead test does not reorder them. To beat a 2-ref/2-insn constant the competing value needs 8+ refs (`floor_log2(refs)*refs`), which means instructions retail does not have, so a 98-99% park on this exact shape is BLOCKED - one `-dg` look settles it and no permuter run helps (1428 iterations, base=25 best=25). Also measured on the way: `code |= K; return fld | code;` 86.76 (the duplicated arms then cross-jump and 2 instructions vanish) and `return fld | code | K;` 85.29 (the third operand moves `code` to `$v1` and reverses the two `or`s).

  **SUPERSEDED IN PART (round 435, the same unit's sibling func_80045B00, re-parked 99.51% at retail's exact 204 bytes / 51 insns): THE TIE IS LIVE_LENGTH-REACHABLE FROM SOURCE, and the price is the `lui`'s position, not the contest.** Hoisting the constant into a named local (`u_long cmd = 0xE4000000;` assigned at the top of the body, `ret = code | cmd;`) DOES hand the masked x `$v0` - `andi v0,a3,0x3ff` and `andi v0,a3,0xfff` come out as retail's - because the long live range (not the ref count) drops the constant's `floor_log2(refs)*refs/live_length` quotient below the value's; what it costs is the materialisation, which moves into the entry block in `$t0` (`or v0,v0,t0`) and leaves the `j` target 4 bytes off: 91.76%. So retail's arrangement needs the constant BOTH short (its `lui` sits one instruction before the `or`) and ranked under the masked x, i.e. a pseudo created late and never hoisted, and no spelling of these two produces both. Also measured on that body: `code |= 0xE4000000; return fld | code;` 99.31 - the `or`'s destination ties to the masked value (`or a0,a0,v0`), which says retail's OR is an in-place update rather than `ret = code | const` with a fresh result, but the constant still takes `$v0`; and the putDispEnv single-expression spelling (`ret = ((y & 0x3FF) << 10) | (x & 0x3FF) | 0xE4000000;` in each arm, no locals) folds the or tree to 50 insns / 200 bytes, 88.82. Third permuter run from the 99.51 body: base=25 best=25, 1953 iterations.
- id: a-mask-load-pair-tie-by-allocno | keywords: regalloc, register, mask, and, prologue, tie, sra
  **A MASK CONSTANT AND A LOADED VALUE COMPETING FOR `$a0` IS DECIDED BY `global_alloc` PRIORITY, NOT BY STATEMENT ORDER: four source orders emitted the identical and/sra sequence** (round 394, battling/main/misc9 func_80082488, parked 99.00% at retail's exact 1016 bytes / 252 insns, 6 differing instructions all in the prologue). Retail: `li a0,-256` then `and a2,t2,a0 / sra t2,t2,8 / and a0,t1,a0 / sra t1,t1,8` (mask in `$a0`, the two coordinates in `$t2`/`$t1`, col/row reusing them in place, mz overwriting the mask register); ours: mask in `$a3`, coordinates in `$v0`/`$a0`, and gcc SINKS the mz `and` below the cell-pointer `addu`. Measured identical (99.00%, 1016 bytes, 252 insns): statement order (mx,mz,col,row), and/sra interleaved (mx,col,mz,row), sra-first (col,row,mx,mz), and all six as initialised declarations - the four produce the same RTL, so the mask constant pseudo and the z load (both n_refs=2) are one `allocno_compare` contest. `try_flags.sh --gcc gcc-2.7.2-cdk-psx` scores 89.19 on the body, so the preset is not the way out, and a 3-minute permuter run (1172 iterations, base 255 best 190) only produced `mz = z;` - it drops the mask, i.e. wrong code - so stop after measuring the statement orders.
- id: a-class-instruction-multiset-usually | keywords: regalloc, register, permuter
  THE 95-99.6% CLASS: the instruction multiset is usually already identical and only an allocator tie-break differs (func_801E6578: 20+ hand-written shapes left it untouched; func_800A0C94: 24 declaration orders byte-identical). Hand iteration does not close these, so do not do it: run the permuter (RULES), which tries thousands of such shapes a minute, and prefer a stub with no parked note for your own writing time.
- id: a-hi-register-tie-has | keywords: regalloc, register, cc1, lui, pointer, addiu, hoist, loop
  THE %hi REGISTER TIE HAS A SOURCE ANSWER (round 273, battle/misc27_b func_800B5DF4 99.82 -> 100.00 at retail's exact 456 bytes). cc1 emits a symbol address as TWO pseudos, (set TEMP (high sym)) + (set D (lo_sum TEMP sym)), and the tied form retail shows ('lui s0,%hi' / 'j' / 'addiu s0,s0,%lo') only happens when local_alloc's combine suggestion can pair TEMP with D, which requires D to be a BLOCK-LOCAL quantity. A pointer variable defined in a branch arm and used after the join is cross-block, so it goes to global_alloc and its %hi always lands in $2. So do not hoist the symbol into a pointer local: take the address AT THE USE SITE ('SetRotMatrix(&g_X);' in each arm, letting cross-jumping merge the identical tails), and the %hi ties to the register retail used. This is the same wall as battle/misc35 func_800C0FAC (retail keeps lui %hi(g_BattleWdsEntry) in $s7 across the loop) - try the use-site spelling there too. The way it was found is worth repeating: tools/scripts/cc1_probe.sh <unit> <snippet.c> - a self-contained snippet compiled with the unit's own cc1 and flags in about ten seconds, which prints the assembly and a PROBE <sym> high=<reg> low=<reg> line per address the snippet takes (tools/scripts/probe_example.c is a worked example) answers a register question in minutes that whole-body iteration cannot.

- id: a-parameter-copy-signedness | keywords: regalloc, register, callee-saved, parameter, signed, local, copy
  **A SAME-WIDTH PARAMETER COPY WITH A DIFFERENT SIGNEDNESS MOVES THE WHOLE FUNCTION'S CALLEE-SAVED ASSIGNMENT** (round 389, menu/main/misc12 func_801E7C50 99.48 -> 100.00 at retail's exact 536 bytes, twin-ported from ShopMenuSetStringPrimitives): with `s32 flags` used directly the loop counter took `$s7` and the attribute word `$s6` (99.61%, same instruction count and bytes); the twin's `u32 attr = flags;` local used at both attribute tests put the attribute word in `$s7` and the counter in `$s6`, which is retail's save order. The copy costs no instruction (gcc coalesces it); the unsigned pseudo changes the allocator's priorities. So when a park note says "index and flags are swapped between $s7 and $fp", port the twin's copy-of-a-parameter with the other signedness before hunting the allocator by hand.

- id: a-qi-parameter-used-after | keywords: regalloc, parameter, u8, stack, frame, copy, qi, spill
  **A `u8` PARAMETER READ AFTER A CALL IS COPIED INTO A FRESH LOCAL SLOT BY gcc 2.6.0 (frame +8, and every later stack local shifts up by 8) WHERE RETAIL READS THE INCOMING HOME SLOT, AND `s32 type` REMOVES THE COPY** (round 400, lib_gear/main/main func_801DF7F4, 71.21 -> 74.31 -> 74.39 at retail's exact 720 bytes / 180 insns). Retail's fill body reads the 5th argument in place - `lbu t0,0x58(sp)` twice, sp+0x10 of a 0x48 frame - and has the loop bound `count` at 0x10(sp); with `u8 type` cc1 opens with `lbu t1,96(sp) / sb t1,16(sp)` and the frame becomes 0x50 with count at 0x18, which mirrors most of the register set. Widening the parameter to `s32 type` (the field it is stored into stays `u8`, so the `sb` survives) drops the copy, restores the 0x48 frame and count at 0x10(sp), and the instruction count and byte size land on retail's exactly. Measure the parameter width before parking any prologue whose only visible sin is an 8-byte frame. The same change is NOT free in a bigger body: on the neighbouring func_801DFAC4 `s32 type` pushed `flag1` (an `andi`ed `u8` local) out of `$fp` into a fresh stack byte, 996 -> 1016 bytes, so try both widths and keep the one with fewer EXTRA instructions, not the higher objdiff percent.
- id: a-declaration-with-initialiser-in-the-loop | keywords: regalloc, register, swapped, lbu, loop, declaration, initialiser
  **A DECLARATION-WITH-INITIALISER INSIDE THE LOOP BODY CLOSES A $v0/$v1 SWAP THAT A SEPARATE ASSIGNMENT STATEMENT CANNOT** (round 391, battling/misc11 func_80087068, 99.75 -> 100.00 at retail's exact 353 instructions / 1412 bytes; three earlier permuter runs, two of them 1702 and 4034 iterations, found nothing). Retail's two fade loops reload a byte and multiply it by 0xFF (`lbu v0,194(s4)` then `sll v1,v0,8 / subu v1,v1,v0 / sra v1,v1,8` - load in $v0, chain in $v1). Three spellings were measured: (1) `scaledFade = arg0->fade; scaledFade *= 0xFF;` as separate statements with the multiply done at the store - retail's exact instruction ORDER but the mirror pair (`lbu v1` / `sll v0,v1,8`), 99.75; (2) the multiply inlined into the store (`r0 = bright + ((arg0->fade * 0xFF) >> 8)`) - retail's exact REGISTER pair but cc1 sinks the load below the two preceding byte stores, 97.73; (3) the load as a `u8 fadeByte = arg0->fade;` DECLARATION (with initialiser, first statement of the block) used by the inlined multiply at the store - exact order AND exact registers, 100.00. So an initialised declaration gives the loaded pseudo a different allocator priority than an assignment to a hoisted local, which is what breaks the tie; the local must be `u8` (an `s32` local holding the same load mirrors the pair again at 99.69). This is the cheap third spelling to measure on any exact-size park whose only residual is a mirrored load/derived-value pair.
- id: a-li-constant-is-a-reload-scratch | keywords: regalloc, reload, scratch, li, lui, ori, delay, nop, reorg, dg
  **A LOOP-PREHEADER `li` OF A LARGE CONSTANT (lui+ori split) IS A RELOAD SCRATCH, NOT AN ALLOCNO - when retail's constant register held a live value across the preceding branch, the nop-in-delay-slot + lui/ori-after-target shape is unreachable from C** (round 397, field/main/misc func_8008BF38, parked 97.81% at 580 vs retail's 584 bytes, one instruction short). Retail loads the 0x14000 copy-end into `$t0`, which still holds `&D_8005A448` live on the fall-through path, so reorg cannot steal the `lui` into the `bne` delay slot (nop there) and the function is one instruction longer; every source spelling gives the constant `$v0` (dead at the branch), so reorg fills the slot and the body is 4 bytes short. `-dg` proves the wall: the constant appears post-allocation as `(set (reg:SI 2 v0) (const_int 65536))` + ior 16384 with NO pseudo number, i.e. reload picked the first free register - no allocno exists whose priority a spelling could move. Measured identical at 97.81: an empty `do {} while (0)` around the copy loop (the a-empty-do-while-block-nudges trick), a two-statement in-place `pEnd = pSrc; pEnd = (PartyDataChunk*)((u8*)pEnd + 0x14000);`, plus the ten spellings and the 4-minute permuter run (best 206 vs base 317) of rounds 395-396; preset screen 2.7.2/cdk/2.6.0 all 97.81 or worse. Park on it, and treat any `li`-register + delay-slot-nop pair difference as BLOCKED without a -dg look. Round 435 addendum on the same function: the size IS reachable - forming `pEnd` from the global load before `pSrc` (`pEnd = (PartyDataChunk*)((u8*)g_FieldSavedFramebuffers[i] + 0x14000); pSrc = g_FieldSavedFramebuffers[i];` before the `pDst` load) gives retail's exact 146 insns / 584 bytes but shifts the whole set - `pSrc` takes `$v0`, the index `$a2`, the constant `$v1` - for 96.58%, and placing `pEnd` between the two global loads is byte-identical to the park. So a matching byte COUNT here is not progress: judge by the register set.

- id: a-call-result-pseudo-never-moves-off-v0 | keywords: move, copy, mask, call, switch, in-place, destructive, probe
  **A VALUE THAT COMES STRAIGHT FROM A CALL AND IS CONSUMED BY A NARROW STORE, TWO MASK STORES AND A SWITCH IS NEVER COPIED OFF `$v0` BY cc1 2.7.2, SO RETAIL'S `move v1,v0` / DESTRUCTIVE IN-PLACE MASKS (`andi v0,v0,0xf`, `andi v1,v1,0xc0`) SHAPE IS UNREACHABLE FROM STATEMENT ORDER** (round 435, field/main/misc func_8008EC30, parked 94.74% at 480 vs retail's 484 bytes, one instruction short). `cc1_probe.sh field/main/misc` on the isolated sequence (call, raw `sh` of the result, `gFlags = v & 0xC0`, `t = v & 0xF`, three-case chain) prints the same `andi $3,$2,0xc0` / `andi $3,$2,0xf` / `sh $2` under four shapes: mask before the store, mask after it, `D_800C3A30 = FieldScriptVMGetArgument(7)` with the masks read back through the global (cse folds them, no reload), and a duplicate `raw = v` local (copy-propagated away). The call-result pseudo keeps `$v0` because local-alloc has nothing else to give it, so every mask lands in `$3` and no copy is emitted; retail only gets the copy because its raw value sits in `$v1` while `$v0` is destroyed by the type mask. What DOES buy the copy in the real function is a volatile store on the raw `D_800C3A30` store (round 322: 492 bytes, 89.25%), which then pins the mask after it. Measure this class with a probe, not with the unit: the answer does not vary with statement order.

- id: a-two-address-add-ties-the-pointer | keywords: regalloc, register, pointer, addu, load, tie
  **A `p = &g_Table[index]` POINTER'S REGISTER IS CHOSEN BY THE ADDRESS-CHAIN LOAD IT IS BUILT FROM, NOT BY ITS OWN 5+ STORES: the MIPS `addsi3` ties operand 0 to operand 1, so the pointer IS the load result's pseudo and the whole tie is one `find_free_reg` decision at that load** (round 397, world_map/main/misc6 func_8007E450 parked 97.97% and func_80080578 94.71%, both at retail's exact 148/136 bytes with the instruction streams identical except that retail keeps the pointer in `$v1` and the last of three loaded globals in `$a2` and 2.7.2 mirrors them). `-da` on the preprocessed unit (`docker run --rm -v"$PWD":/w -w /w --platform=linux/amd64 ethos:latest`, the unit's cc1 + `cc_flags` + `-O2 -G8 -da`) prints `;; Register dispositions: 73 in 6 75 in 4 76 in 6 77 in 3 78 in 5 ...` and `;; Hard regs used: 2 3 4 5 6`, i.e. the address+load pseudo takes `$a2` and only skips `$a0` because the `index` parameter still holds it at the load's birth - retail's `$v1` is not the next candidate in any order a spelling moves, and `-G0` (94.71), gcc-2.6.0 (48.62) and 2.7.2-cdk (92.03) do not reach it either. Measured dead on the same body: the three value locals declared in reverse, the chained `posX = posZ = *(u32 *)g_...;` alias, and an `int nResult = 1;` before the last store to keep the return's `li` out of the `jr` delay slot (gcc folds it, 94.71 unchanged). When the SAME mirror appears in two functions of one unit (these are near-copies of each other), it is one allocator decision - park both after the `-da` look instead of permuting, and note that these two parks can be unparked with `park.py` only when the body block is `/*` on its own line followed by the definition at column 0.

- id: a-redeclare-scalar-as-array-272 | keywords: lui, addiu, rematerialise, saved, register, extern, array, asm-label
  **The asm-label array re-declaration also works on cc1 2.7.2 (Default preset), not only 2.6.0.** `extern s16 g_X[] __asm__("g_X"); pMode = g_X; switch (*pMode)` kept retail's `lui s0,%hi` / `addiu s0,%lo` and all three `lh`/`sh` through `$s0` across a `jal`, where the scalar, `s16 *p = &g_X`, and the bare `switch (g_X)` each rematerialised `lui/addiu` or the maspsx `$at` macro per use (round 411, field/camera/camera_movement func_8008FC4C: 63.33 -> 82.87 on that edit alone). The same round measured the case-tree half of the family on the same function: a switch with only two case nodes roots the tree at the LOWER value (`beqz x,case0` then `beq x,1,case1`), while adding a third label whose body equals the switch's default (`case 2: return; default: return;` with the shared tail after the switch) roots it at the middle value and reproduces retail's longer `beq x,1 / slti x,2 / beqz END / nop / bnez END` chain, the third node emitting no instruction at all.

  Round 420, world_map/main/misc1 func_80071EF0 (63 insns, 252 bytes = retail): the alias also moves a **single `sh` store**, with no callee-saved register involved. Retail writes queue entry 0's index with `sh v1,0(a0)` - the $a0 already holding `&g_WorldMapStreamQueue` for the following call - while the scalar decl, `(StreamDataQueueEntry*)&g_WorldMapStreamQueue`, an `s32` local and a volatile lvalue all fold the store into the maspsx `$at` macro (`lui $at,%hi` + `sh v1,%lo($at)`, one instruction over, 97.37%). `extern StreamDataQueueEntry g_WorldMapStreamQueueEntries[] __asm__("g_WorldMapStreamQueue"); pQueue = g_WorldMapStreamQueueEntries;` gives retail's 252 bytes with the register store, and the reloc name is unchanged. **A second symbol_addrs line for an address that already has a name is REJECTED by splat 0.33 - `error reading config/symbol_addrs.<bin>.txt: Duplicate symbol detected!` and the whole build stops - so the alias must be spelled in the C declaration, never as a second name in the file.**
- id: a-an-asm-label-array-extern | keywords: regalloc, callee-saved, rematerialise, address, symbol, extern, lui
  **A SCALAR GLOBAL'S ADDRESS CANNOT BE KEPT IN A CALLEE-SAVED REGISTER ACROSS A CALL - it is a rematerialisable constant, so cc1 2.6.0 re-forms `lui/addiu` at every use site; declaring the SAME symbol a second time as an unbounded ARRAY with an asm label makes the address a real value and it lands in a saved register** (round 397, battle/main/misc2 func_80076710, 95.90 -> 97.94% and retail's `lui s0,%hi` / `addiu s0,%lo` / two `lw a3,0(s0)` reproduced exactly). Retail holds `&g_BattlePrimStartIndex` in `$s0` across two `func_80025FA8` calls; every local-only spelling rematerialised the address per site at 95.90% - a `s32 *pCtx` pointer local, `mem_addr primStart = (mem_addr)&g_X;` plus `*(s32 *)primStart`, and `&g_X` passed inline. What works without touching the shared declaration (which other matched functions read as a scalar) is a second declaration with an asm label: `extern s32 g_BattlePrimStartIndexTable[] __asm__("g_BattlePrimStartIndex");` then `pCtx = g_BattlePrimStartIndexTable;`. The reloc name stays the symbol's, so objdiff and the link are unaffected, and `*pCtx` still reloads through the register (which is what retail's two `lw a3,0(s0)` are). Measured in a `cc1_probe.sh` snippet on the same unit: the array form emits one `lui/addiu` plus `lw $7,0($18)` per call with `$18` saved in the prologue, the scalar form `lw $7,g_ProbeScalar` at every site.
  MEASURED NEGATIVE, round 411, field/main/main func_80077AB4: neither the plain unbounded-array extern nor this asm-label alias fires when the pointer's only use in its own block is the guard load (`pCount = table; if (*pCount == 0) return;`) and the address has to be live again after the calls for two displacement derivations (`D_801E8644 = pCount - 10;`, `pScratch = (u8*)pCount - 0x78;`) - both forms fold the loads back to `lui v0,%hi` + `lw v0,0(v0)` and re-form a CALLER-saved register per derivation (0x40/t0 before and after each call), while retail has one `lui/addiu` into $s0 in the prologue and `lw 0(s0)` for both guards. Also measured identical: the early-`return` guard vs the FieldFree `if (*pCount != 0) { ... }` block shape (FieldFree DOES keep its `la $16`), hoisting the displacement local above the guard, -G0 (65.49), cdk -G0 (60.94). So the family is context-sensitive: a load-only use in the block where the pointer is assigned still folds even through an array decl.

- id: a-callee-saved-param-loses-to-nothing | keywords: regalloc, callee-saved, parameter, pointer, constant, priority, dg, da, unreachable
  **A CALLEE-SAVED PARAMETER POINTER WITH 6 REFS ACROSS THE WHOLE FUNCTION CANNOT BE MADE TO LOSE `$s0` TO A 3-REF CONSTANT, so a retail body that parks the pointer in `$s2` and the constant in `$s0` is not reachable from this C at all** (round 397, battle/main/misc19 func_8009B46C, parked 97.59% at retail's exact 536 bytes / 134 insns). Retail keeps the `u16*` parameter in `$s2`, `drop` in `$s1` and `limit` in `$s0` (its `ori s0,zero,0xa` even sits in a delay slot); 2.7.2 gives pointer=`$s0`, drop=`$s1`, limit=`$s2`. The `-da` numbers (lreg: `Register 72 used 6 times across 97 insns ... pointer` = the param, `135 used 3 times across 25 insns` = drop, `137 used 3 times across 34 insns` = limit) give priorities 2*6/97*4 = 0.495, 0.48 and 0.353, so the hand-out is forced; retail's order would need limit > drop > arg0, i.e. the 3-ref constant outranking the 6-ref pointer - and shrinking the pointer's ref count is impossible because its own double `lhu` (the volatile re-read) and both stores are retail's shape. Measured dead: `u8 drop` (moves nothing at all, still 97.59 at 536 bytes - the QI size term does not matter because it loses to the pointer either way), and gcc-2.6.0-psx via try_flags is 97.81 (it fixes the mfhi md shape but not this contest), cdk 94.63. Verdict on sight: a mirrored `$s0`/`$s2` pair where one side is the entry parameter used through to the tail is a park-after-one-dump case.

- id: a-bitfield-merge-needs-adjacency | keywords: regalloc, bitfield, merge, adjacent, rmw, store, split, arm, uninit, permuter
  **A MULTI-BITFIELD RMW MERGES ONLY ACROSS ADJACENT STATEMENTS, AND RETAIL'S TWO-REGISTER ARM TAILS CAN ONLY BE REPRODUCED BY AN UNINITIALISED POINTER - so both are walls, not spellings** (round 398, slus_006.64/system/work_list TimerWorkListAddTask, parked 84.20% at 232 bytes vs retail's 240/60 insns). Measured: (1) `pEntry->unk14 = pData->unk10; _1=0; _2=0; _3=0;` adjacent merges into ONE lw/and-chain/sw with the three clears one and at a time; putting any statement between the value-assign and the clears (e.g. the sibling word's `unk10 = id`) splits it into TWO stores of the same word (58.70 vs 84.20). (2) The read that feeds the chain must sit right after the first store in source (`unk0 = pData; unk14 = pData->unk10; ...` hoists `lw a0,0x10(a0)` next to retail's; moving the group after the callbacks drops it late and costs 23%). (3) Retail's flag arms write through DIFFERENT registers ($t3 taken, $v1 else, a copy made in the beqz delay slot), which is why cross-jumping leaves retail's two arm-tail stores separate while gcc merges ours into one shared tail - and a 4-min permuter run found exactly one better body (base 956 best 806) whose edit was `new_var2->unk14_3 = 1` with new_var2 UNINITIALISED, i.e. a second pointer pseudo gcc cannot prove equal to pEntry. Rejected as wrong code; the honest spelling cannot break the cross-jump. Preset ruled out: 2.6.0 79.7, 2.7.2-cdk 83.72 vs 84.20 under unit flags. Same sched2 wall as the sibling WorkListAddTask (89.34 ceiling, 12 orderings).

- id: b-postdecrement-on-a-struct-array | keywords: addu, operand, addiu, minus, one, 255, decrement, u8, array
  **`p->byteArray[slot]--` ON A STRUCT FIELD EMITS `addiu reg,reg,-1` AND BASE-FIRST `addu`, where `*(u8 *)((u8 *)p + slot + K) -= 1` emits the SAME arithmetic as `addu reg,reg,255` and INDEX-FIRST** (round 397, battle/main/misc2 func_800765C4, 99.99 -> 100.00 at retail's exact 332 bytes). The two spellings differ only in how the truncating store meets the constant: through a `u8 *` the whole subtraction is folded to `(x + 255)` (equal mod 256), through a u8 struct member cc1 keeps the SImode `-1`; and the member form also fixes the `addu` operand order the earlier park had solved with a separate embedded-assignment temp. Probed on the unit with `cc1_probe.sh`, one spelling per function: `->mode[slot] -= 1`, `= x - 1`, `--` and a `s8 *` all give `-1`; `u8 *p; p[slot + K] -= 1` gives 255; `s32 m = p[slot]; p[slot] = m - 1;` gives `-1` with base-first. So a diff whose ONLY residual is `addiu v0,v0,255` against retail's `addiu v0,v0,-1` is a typing question, not arithmetic: find the struct member the byte belongs to.

symptoms: addu operands swapped, index-first vs base-first
try: addu operand index-first base-first
- id: b-row-index-local-sums-slot-inline | keywords: addu, operand, destination, tie, index, local, stride, table
  **WHEN THE ONLY RESIDUAL IS THE ADDRESS `addu`'s DESTINATION ON A `row * 0x28 + slot * STRIDE` TABLE INDEX, GIVE THE ROW INDEX ITS OWN LOCAL AND SUM THE SLOT TERM INLINE IN THE ADDRESS** - `rowOff = arg2 * 2 + arg1 + 0x16; flags = *(u16 *)(g_BattleCellActionRows + rowOff * 0x28 + arg0 * 0x5F0);` (round 432, battle/main/misc17 func_8009187C, 99.89 -> 100.00 at retail's exact 700 bytes / 175 insns, XenoBattle260 = gcc 2.6.0). One `off = (row) * 0x28 + slot * 0x5F0;` temp emits `addu v0,v1,v0` where retail has `addu v1,v1,v0` (the sum's destination on the row-chain register). Measured dead at that same 700 bytes: the plain `off` temp (99.89), the double negation `(row) * 0x28 - (-(slot * 0x5F0))` (99.89 - no pointer base to legitimise, so it folds away), an embedded assignment of the whole sum (99.89) or of the row term only (99.77), `rowOff`/`slotOff` locals summed into `off` (99.69), `rowOff += slot` (99.69), swapping the C operand order (96.17 - the two multiply chains also change emit order), no local at all (89.55; cc1 folds `0x16 * 0x28` into the displacement), a pointer local for the base (89.14), and three declaration orders of the three locals (byte-identical). The named row local is what keeps the row chain its own pseudo, so local-alloc's tie takes it.
- id: b-inplace-flips-pointer-base-but-hoists-mem-base | keywords: addu, operand, in-place, cse, hoist, pointer, array, global
  **THE IN-PLACE `+=` FORM FLIPS `addu` ORDER FOR A POINTER-VARIABLE BASE, BUT MAKES cc1 2.6.0 HOIST A MEM-BASE'S CONSTANT ADDRESS.** round 393, battle/main/misc23 func_800A5EB4 (356 insns, retail's exact 1424 bytes): 30 `base[off + K]` byte copies where retail has `addu v0,v0,a1` (base first) and the bare subscript emits `addu v0,a1,v0`. The 15 sites whose base is `g_BattleSpriteRenderData` (a plain `u8 *` global, one load from one symbol) reach retail's order with two statements per store - `pSlot = g_BattleSpriteRenderData; pSlot += i * 0x24; pDst[0] = pSlot[0x1740];` - 99.16 -> 99.58 with every other instruction, the frame and both sizes unchanged. The 15 sites whose base is `g_BattlePartPrimBuffers[0]` (a MEM at a CONSTANT address) cannot use it: any spelling that hands that base or its sum to a register (`pSlot = g_BattlePartPrimBuffers[0]; pSlot += ...`, or the embedded `*((pSlot = g_BattlePartPrimBuffers[0]) + i * 0x18 + K)`) makes cse materialise the array's address once (`lui t0,%hi` + `addiu t0,%lo` before both loops) and reload through it - 92.95% at 1368 bytes - and converting even 3 of the 15 sites triggers it (352 insns, 97.32%). Plain spellings that keep the address inside the MEM preserve retail's count but stay index-first: the plain subscript, and `*(g_BattlePartPrimBuffers[0] + i * 0x18 + K)`. So for a MEM-with-constant-address base, base-first `addu` and per-site `lui/lw` are mutually exclusive in gcc 2.6.0; park instead of hunting. The same conversion applies to that unit's parked func_800A64E4 (98.71%, identical shape). Round 396 measured the same trap for a MEM base that is a FIELD of a global pointer: in battle/main/misc17 `pRec = g_BattleGraphicsWork->pPacketPrims; pRec += idx * 0x28;` scores 83.52 (696 bytes, +4) where `pPacketPrims + idx * 0x28` scores 97.72 -- the `+=` spelling hoists the pool load here too, and the only spelling that moved the second sum in place was the `prims - (-(idx * 0x28))` double negation the unit's matched neighbours already use (97.72 -> 97.83).
- id: c-inplace-flips-the-order-but-mirrors-the-pointer-and-byte | keywords: addu, operand, inplace, base-first, v0, v1, mirror, probe
  **THE IN-PLACE FORM BUYS BASE-FIRST `addu` ON A STORE-SIDE COPY TOO, BUT THE BODY THEN SITS ON A WHOLE-FUNCTION `$v0`/`$v1` MIRROR THAT SCORES LOWER THAN THE INDEX-FIRST PARK - so "the twin matched at 100 with this spelling" is not evidence for the write side** (round 433, battle/main/misc23 func_800A64E4, re-parked 98.71%; the in-place version of the identical body is 93.73% at retail's exact 928 bytes / 232 instructions). All 30 sites take retail's `addu vN,vN,a2` / `addu vN,vN,a3` with `pSlot = g_...; pSlot += i * 0x24; pSlot[K] = pSrc[N];` over the SCALAR extern, and the read twin BattleInitColorTintTable (same file, 100.00) is the same 30 sites in the other direction - but on the write side this cc1 keeps the reloaded pointer in `$v1` and the `lbu` result in `$v0` where retail has pointer `$v0` / byte `$v1`, at every site (only the two blocks' first store also shows a `v0`->`v1` copy). `cc1_probe.sh battle/main/misc23` on a standalone three-store loop reproduces the same mirror (`addu $3,$3,$5` / `lbu $2,...`), so it is local-alloc's scan on the qty pair and not the unit's surround; dead on it: swapping the `pSrc`/`pSlot` declarations (byte-identical) and a 3-minute two-job permuter run (RESULT NONE, no change to the pair). Keep the index-first park; on a body where the destination is a global, port the twin's spelling only if you are prepared to trade the operand order for the register pair.
- id: c-no-index-spelling-flips-a-store-side-addu-under-a-reloaded-global | keywords: addu, operand, index-first, base-first, reloc, reload, giv, store, probe, unreachable
  **ON A STORE THROUGH A GLOBAL POINTER THAT MUST BE RELOADED PER STATEMENT, EVERY INDEX-SIDE SPELLING EMITS THE SAME INDEX-FIRST `addu` - typed struct, row-array type, user offset variable, and BOTH embedded-assignment tricks included - so the `b-embedded-assignment-temp-for-addu-order` lever does not transfer to this shape and the park is closed** (round 446, battle/main/misc23 func_800A64E4, re-parked 98.71% at retail's exact 928 bytes / 232 insns, XenoBattle260Div = gcc-2.6.0 -G0). The body is 30 `base[i * 0x24 or 0x18 + K] = pSrc[n]` byte copies whose base must reload per store (a store through it may alias the global), retail emitting `addu v0,v0,a2` (pointer in `$v0` first, byte in `$v1`). objdiff lists exactly 60 differing lines, all that operand pair. Six spellings probed in one file with `tools/scripts/cc1_probe.sh battle/main/misc23 tools/scripts/probe_misc23_addu.c`: `g[i * 0x24 + K]`, `((ProbeSlot *)(g + 0x1740))[i].c0[k]` (a 0x24-byte struct with 8-byte colour slots), `((u8 (*)[0x24])(g + 0x1740))[i][k]`, `g[off + K]` with `off` a LOCAL the loop increments by 0x24, `g[(off = i * 0x24) + K]`, and `((u8 *)(p = g + i * 0x24))[K]` - all six are byte-identical: `lw $2,g` / `lbu $3,...` / `addu $2,$5,$2` / `sb $3,5952($2)` with the biv in `$5`. So cc1 2.6.0 canonicalises the strength-reduced biv in front of the reloaded pointer no matter what the source says (loop.c rewrites the address around the giv), and the two documented flips are properties of a POINTER-VARIABLE base or a LOCAL-ARRAY slot, not of a reloaded global. Also measured on this body: spelling the 15 `g_BattlePartPrimBuffers[0][K]` sites over the SCALAR extern (`g_BattlePartPrimBuffers[K]`, no `[0]`) silently turns the guard `!= NULL` into a BYTE test and costs 2 instructions / 8 bytes (234 insns, 936 bytes, 95.65%) - keep an array-view alias (`extern u8 *g[2] __asm__("g")`) when the guard tests element 0.
- id: b-in-place-addu-flips-but-loads-base-early | keywords: addu, operand, in-place, pointer, regalloc, tie
  **An IN-PLACE `+=` is the only spelling measured that puts the destination of the address `addu` on the BASE pseudo, and it moves where the base is loaded.** round 392, menu/main/misc7_r2 func_801D5ED4 (gcc 2.6.0; parked 99.32 at retail's exact 704 bytes / 176 instructions). Retail coalesces the `base + renderContext * 0x28` sum with the base and this build with the offset, at four sites (two palette-store addresses and the two POLY_FT4 field pairs). `pBase = (u8*)base + ctx * 0x28;`, a cast-over-subscript `((POLY_FT4*)base)[ctx]`, an embedded `(q = base + off)` inside the load, and both C operand orders all emit the SAME mirrored `addu off,off,base`. Only `pBase = base; pBase += ctx * 0x28;` gives retail's `addu base,base,off` - but two statements make cc1 load the base at the assignment, so in the branch arm where the offset chain still occupies `$v1` the base takes a fresh `$a2` instead of retail's late load into the reused `$v1` (98.32% at the same 704 bytes), and one `s32 ctxOffset = ctx * 0x28;` local shared by two blocks is hoisted into a callee-saved `$s5` across the calls (89.83% at 680). So use the in-place form when the operand ORDER is the only residual, and check the base's load position in the diff afterwards.
- id: b-5x-scale-has-two | keywords: addu, operand
  **A 1.5x scale has to be TWO statements**: `stat = stat + (u32)stat * 2;` then `stat = stat >> 1;`. Written as one expression (`stat * 3 / 2`, or `(stat << 1) + stat`) the commutative add comes out in the other operand order and parks at 99.95 at retail's exact size. Do not tidy it back.
- id: b-pointer-local-holding-base | keywords: addu, operand, cast, pointer, u8
  **A pointer local holding the base SEPARATELY from the offset sum is how you get retail's base-vs-index `addu` order.** round 315: `pBase = (u8*)g_Menu->pGearWindowPrims; pEntry = pBase + offset;` emits `addu v1,a0,v1` where every single-expression spelling canonicalises to index-first (99.90 -> 100.00). The converse is equally true and just as useful: a CAST over an index-first sum (`((LINE_F3*)(i * sizeof(LINE_F3) + (u32)base))->f`) is what a fully typed subscript cannot do, because C subscript syntax forces base-first. So the `addu` order is a choice between three spellings, not a wall - measure all three before parking.
- id: b-embedded-assignment-temp-for-addu-order | keywords: addu, operand, index-first, base-first, embedded assignment, temp, permuter
