  **A throwaway temp written INSIDE the address expression is the fourth spelling that flips `addu` order, and the only one that survives when the base must be reloaded per statement.** round 391, menu/misc12 func_801E733C (gcc 2.6.0, 236 insns): the last two stores of a 16-store loop wanted retail's `addu a2,a2,v1` / `sh v0,0x2792(a2)` and every plain spelling gave `addu v1,v1,a2` - probed with cc1_probe: `(u32)base + idx + c` (v1), `(u8*)base + idx + c` (v2), a `u32`/`u8*` local holding the BASE (v3/v4, spills to `$s`), index-first (v5) and a `POLY_FT4` subscript (v6) all fold to index-first, and the subscript form also materialises the address. `((POLY_FT4*)((u8*)g_Menu->pPrimBuffer + (idx = i * 2 + g_Menu->renderContext) * 0x28) + 0x277C)->tpage = GetTPage(...)` - the assignment embedded in the address - gives base-first at 99.92 (one store), and the same trick on the second store reached 100.00 at 944 bytes. The permuter found it (base=40 best=20, 4555 iters) and it is semantically inert, so it is not the usual wrong-code candidate; keep it with a one-line why comment and report the function ROUGH. A local assigned in its own statement instead gets hoisted (the base load is loop-invariant) and costs a callee-saved `$s6` + 24 frame bytes, 87-90%.
- id: b-embedded-assignment-in-a-store-address-for-a-local-array | keywords: addu, operand, embedded assignment, array, stack, slot, base-first
  **The embedded-assignment temp also flips the base/index `addu` order for a LOCAL ARRAY slot, and it is the only spelling that keeps the address formation AFTER the call.** round 396, battle/main/misc3 func_80076EA4 (gcc 2.7.2, 464 bytes / 116 insns, MATCH). Retail's `sll s0,s2,0x2; addiu s5,sp,0x10; jal BattleAllocTextBuffer; addu s0,s5,s0; sw a0,0x0(s0)` is base-first and forms the address after the call. `pBuf[i] = BattleAllocTextBuffer(0x1B);` gives index-first `addu s0,s0,s5` (98.19%); a separate `pSlot = pBuf + i;` statement gives retail's order but forms the address BEFORE the call, so `li a0,0x1B` falls out of the jal delay slot (468 bytes, 90.78%); `*(pSlot = pBuf + i) = BattleAllocTextBuffer(0x1B);` gives both retail's `addu` order and its post-call placement, 100.00 at retail's 464 bytes.
- id: a-offset-giv-vs-symbol-fold-is-a-cse-tie | keywords: regalloc, addu, offset, giv, cse, symbol, fold, tie, displacement
  **When several memory uses share an offset that cc1 knows is `base_giv + const`, it folds the constant into ONE symbol address and uses the base giv with a displacement for the rest, instead of keeping the single offset giv retail has** - so an otherwise instruction-identical body loses on the symbol/addend pair, not on any source spelling. round 396, battle/main/misc3 func_8007819C (372 bytes / 93 insns = retail exactly, correct addresses, parked 98.39%): retail keeps `0x60 + 0xC0k` in one register and uses it for `lui at,%hi(D_800D36C0); addu at,at,s3; sw %lo(at)`, the same off `g_BattleTextSlotBuffers`, and `addu v0,s3,s5`; the compile keeps `0xC0k` there and rewrites the two stores to `%lo(D_800D3720)` (addend 0) and `g_BattleTextSlotBuffers+96`. Writing the sum as `(i+1)*0x60` or as an `off` local assigned in the loop is identical; `off = 0x60` before the loop with `off += 0xC0` at the bottom DOES give retail's shape for both stores but needs a 7th saved register (384 bytes, 90.80), and a second BIV `n = i + 1` costs 103 insns. Park, do not re-brief.
- id: b-inline-the-prim-address | keywords: addu, operand, pointer, cast, poly, permuter
  **A named pointer local can COST you the addu order it is supposed to buy - inline the cast into the call arguments when it does.** round 389, menu/misc7_r2 func_801D74EC (gcc 2.6.0): `pPrim = (POLY_FT4*)((u8*)base + primOffset + 0x11D0); ... pPrim->x0` parked at 99.91% with `addu v1,v1,a0` where retail has `addu v1,a0,v1`, and the same sum written inline in the `MenuSetQuadVertices(...)` arguments - repeated seven times, gcc CSEs it - gives retail's order at 100.00%. Adding `pBase`/`pEntry` locals (the b-pointer-local lever that closed round 315 in the same file!) did flip the operand order but also moved g_Menu from `$a0` to `$v1` in both loop preheaders, 97.24%. The permuter found the shape (base=20 best=10, 1391 iters); it does not fix the readability, so report the function ROUGH.
- id: b-accumulator-assignment-keeps-early | keywords: addu, operand, slti, xori
  **An accumulator assignment keeps an early compare in place**: `far |= dz >= 0x800;` leaves the dx compare (`slti`+`xori`) right after the `negu` and before the dz loads, where `farX | farZ` and an inline `(dx >= 0x800) | (dz >= 0x800)` both sink it past the dz compare.
- id: b-redefining-value-already-dead | keywords: addu, operand, addiu, cc1, frame, mult
  **Redefining a value INTO an already-dead local changes how the folder schedules the arithmetic** (round 326): `roll = (flags = rand() % 100 - diff);` (with `flags` dead after its last use) made cc1 place the `addiu` after the remainder and took the body 99.54 -> 99.78, where a separate `limit` local lands it in the divider's post-mult slots and the inline form gets re-associated. A NEW local costs a frame slot; reusing a dead one is free. It also hides the real value behind an unrelated name, so say so in a one-line comment.
- id: b-place-compare-accumulator-emits | keywords: addu, operand, slti, xori
  **An in-place compare accumulator emits retail's `slti`/`xori` shapes**: `dx = dx >= 0x800; ... dz = dz >= 0x800; ... dx |= dz;` writes each compare back into the distance's own register, where `far = dx >= 0x800; far |= dz >= 0x800;` splits every compare through `$v0` and costs instructions (round 328, world_map/misc8). Same family as the `far |= dz >= 0x800;` accumulator lever.
- id: b-shift-has-stay-separate | keywords: addu, operand, sll
  **A shift has to stay a separate operand**: `offset = GetArgument(2);` and then `(offset << 9)` at each use, because writing `offset = GetArgument(2) << 9` lets gcc fold the copy into the `sll` and the body comes out 4 bytes short (round 330, field/misc func_800871B0 97.81 -> 99.59).
- id: b-try-i-vs-i | keywords: addu, operand
  Try `++i` vs `i++` and swapping operands of commutative ops when only instruction order differs.
- id: b-addu-operand-order-follows | keywords: addu, operand, array
  addu operand order follows C order: (i + p->array)->field emits index-first addu, p->array[i].field does not.
- id: b-opposite-side-spelling-lever | keywords: addu, operand, u8, array, cast, lbu, lui, macro
  THE OPPOSITE-SIDE SPELLING LEVER (round 170, battle/misc11 func_800826CC, 84.29 -> 96.00): two references to the same global get SEPARATE 'lui $at / addu $at / lbu %lo(at)' macro forms only when their RTL differs - write one as '*(u8 *)(off + (u32)sym)' and the other as '*(u8 *)((u32)sym + off)', with the index a NAMED local. Inline index expressions, array-vs-cast, volatile and pointer locals all canonicalise into one register and come out 8 bytes short.
- id: b-embedded-assignment-lever-strongest | keywords: addu, operand, u8, cse
  THE EMBEDDED-ASSIGNMENT LEVER (the strongest one for addu operand order): retail emits the BASE-FIRST 'addu d,base,offset' only when that sum is the RESULT OF AN ASSIGNMENT. As a plain sub-expression gcc always canonicalises to offset-first, whatever the C operand order ('(u8*)base + off', 'base[off]', '(u32)base + off', offset-first, bracket forms - all measured identical). So write '*((pSlot = (u8*)base + off) + K) += x' or hand the sum to a local first ('pCounter = base + off; pCounter[K] = 0;'), and keep the embedded locals SEPARATE - reusing one makes gcc CSE them and the address stops being re-derived. It closed menu/misc11_r2's func_801E6B70 from 99.49 after two workers had called the five addu orders uncontrollable.
- id: b-one-offset-local-per | keywords: addu, cse, offset, local, prim, subscript
  **THE SAME `index * K + CONST` ADDRESS AT N SITES NEEDS N LOCALS.** round 391, battle/main/misc2 func_80073FB8 (186 instructions, 744 bytes) went 99.84 -> 99.95 -> 100.00 purely by giving the three odd sites their own `u32 primOfs`/`slotPrimOfs`/`primOfs2` locals instead of sharing one: with ONE shared local only the prologue site gets it right, because there the sum coalesces into the call's argument register ($a1) and prints retail's offset-first `addu a1,a1,v1`; the two mid-function sites then put the sum in $v0 and the base in $a1 (95.45%). One local per site (each assigned immediately before its own AddPrim) makes every site accumulate in $a1. The offset-first C form `AddPrim(ot, (u8 *)(primOfs + (u32)g_BattleGraphicsWork))` is what retail's order needs, and every no-local spelling (`(u8 *)(index * 12 + K + (u32)base)`, a `mem_addr base` statement, a compound `primOfs += (u32)base`) reassociates the constant onto the base and loses 4-5 instructions - so measure "one local per site" before concluding a base-vs-offset `addu` order is unreachable. Same mechanism as a-two-similar-blocks-must2, applied to a repeated offset sum rather than to a pointer.
- id: b-base-statement-lever-round | keywords: addu, operand, addiu, cc1, lui, u16
  THE BASE-IN-A-STATEMENT LEVER (round 149, battle/misc5 func_8007A92C, 84.67 -> 100): when retail materialises a global base with 'lui/addiu' AFTER the loads of the index words instead of above them, declare the base 'mem_addr base;' and ASSIGN it ('base = (mem_addr)D_800D3420;') - a declaration initializer makes cc1 fold the address above the loads. Then write the final address INDEX-FIRST, '((u16 *)((p[1] << 1) + (arg1 * 0x40 + base)))', which gives retail's 'addu' destination and operand order; base-last spellings canonicalise back.
- id: b-element-pointer-local-lever | keywords: addu, operand, lbu, pointer, permuter
  THE ELEMENT-POINTER LOCAL LEVER: a tie of the form retail 'addu v1,v0,v1 / lbu v0,K(v1)' (element address in the BASE) against yours 'addu v0,v0,v1 / lbu v0,K(v0)' (sum coalesced into the scaled INDEX) on a 'table[idx].field' access is closed by giving the ELEMENT a pointer local - 'pElem = &table[idx]; ... pElem->field' - ONE SITE AT A TIME, and it is the opposite of what an old note in this repo claimed. It closed shop_menu's func_801CE480 from 99.71 (four sites; the two accessory loops are coupled, so flipping only one leaves the other tied) after a permuter run from the same body found nothing.
- id: b-global-plus-index-retail | keywords: addu, operand, permuter
  A global plus an index that retail addresses with two chained `addu` (`addu v1,v1,v0` then `addu at,v1,at`): split the base over two statements (`pFlags = D_800C3EA4; pFlags = pFlags + off;`). Any single-expression form gives one `addu` and 99.8% (round 56, battle/misc1 func_80073380, found by the permuter).
- id: b-commutative-sum-will-canonicalise | keywords: addu, operand, cast
  A COMMUTATIVE SUM THAT WILL NOT CANONICALISE (round 126, closed a park round 110 had dated as a wall): when retail has `addu s0,s4,s6` and every spelling gives `addu s0,s6,s4`, do not reorder operands - COPY one parameter into a variable first and sum from the variable: `dy = arg2;` before any other use, then `(s16)(dy + arg4 - 8)` instead of `(s16)(arg2 + arg4 - 8)`. The explicit cast form is required (`dy += arg4 - 8` is 4 bytes bigger and 97.05%), and the copy is what stops gcc folding the two parameters into one commutative pair. The same trick applies wherever a park note blames an addu operand order that survives every source spelling.
- id: b-pdst-base-pdst-i | keywords: addu, operand, u8
  'pDst = base; *(pDst + i + K)' emits a base-first addu where 'p[i + k]' never does; '((u8*)base + i)[K]' keeps the constant offset last.
- id: b-three-statement-shape-levers | keywords: addu, operand, loop, addiu, li, lui, move, nop
  THREE STATEMENT-SHAPE LEVERS FROM ROUNDS 291-292, each measured on a closed match. (a) **Two separate if statements, not if (...) {...} else if (...) {...}**, when retail FALLS THROUGH from the first test's body into the second test: with else if gcc emits j+nop where retail falls through (battle/misc17's BattleRestoreSlotConditionFlags, 448 bytes / 100.00 only in the separate-if form; both forms are semantically identical there because the two conditions are exclusive). (b) **A loop counter's initialiser belongs as its own statement BEFORE the for**, not in the init clause: n1 = 0; then for (i = 0; i < n1; ...). This is the opposite of menu/main/misc3's for (i = 0, matched = 1; ...) lever, where a ONCE-INITIALISED INVARIANT must sit in the init clause to get retail's move a1,zero / li t1,1 order - so: a counter declared before the loop, an invariant inside it. (c) **&g_Array[i] built in TWO statements** (pp = g_Array; pSlot = &pp[i];) emits lui/addiu %hi before the sll where the one-statement form puts it after: 98.80 vs 91.39 (battle/misc23).
- id: b-index-first-subscript-pointer | keywords: addu, operand, pointer, s32
  AN INDEX-FIRST SUBSCRIPT ON A POINTER REPRODUCES RETAIL'S OFFSET-FIRST addu (round 300, menu/misc8_r2): (i * 2 + pCur->renderContexts[i])[g_Menu->pCursors->polysCursor].x0 = ... gives addu v0,v0,a0 where the ordinary polysCursor[...] subscript gives the commuted addu a0,a0,v0 - it fixed eight sites at once (87 -> 90.5 percent on that body). The same trick appears as (s32 *)((u32)((row << 7) + col) * 4 + (u32)g_BattleGridCells) in battling/misc9.
- id: b-do-generalise-winning-spelling | keywords: addu, operand, loop, pointer
  DO NOT GENERALISE A WINNING SPELLING ACROSS SITES IN ONE FUNCTION (round 294). menu/main/misc9's func_801DD5E8: replacing a panel-pointer local with the inline g_Menu->unk430 spelling was worth +5.5 points at both call sites, but re-applying the same edit inside loop 2 regresses 99.58 -> 95.19, and lengthening that local's live range at the other site regresses to 99.34. Each site's register assignment is decided independently; re-measure per site.
- id: b-second-lever-same-class | keywords: addu, operand, delay, jal, s32
  THE SECOND LEVER FOR THE SAME CLASS IS OPERAND ASSOCIATION (round 289): retail often emits '(product + constant) + base' where gcc normalises to '(base + constant) + product'. Only writing the constant against the base - 'p[0x66A] * 40 + (0x320 + (s32)p)' - keeps retail's 'addu a1,a1,v1' in the jal delay slot.

- id: a-addu-index-first-is-the-legitimiser | keywords: addu, operand, order, address, reload, base-first, probe, hedged
  **Which side of an address `addu` the base lands on is decided by this cc1's address legitimisation, not by the C - every spelling gives INDEX-first** (round 394, battle/misc23 func_800A64E4, parked 98.71% at retail's exact 928 bytes / 232 instructions with the 30 `addu v0,v0,a2` vs `addu v0,a2,v0` operands as the ONLY residual). Measured with `tools/scripts/cc1_probe.sh battle/main/misc23 <snippet.c>` on a standalone copy of the loop (the cheap way to answer an order question: the unit file is never touched): `g[i * 0x24 + K]`, `*(u8 *)(g + i * 0x24 + K)`, `((u8 *)(g + i * 0x24))[K]`, `((u8 *)(off + (s32)g))[K]` with an s32 offset variable, the offset written FIRST (`i * 0x24 + (s32)g + K`) and a struct view `((Row *)g)[i].c0[0]` all compile to `addu $2,$4,$2`. The one shape that emits base-first (`pDst = &g[i * 0x24 + K]`) keeps K in the sum, so it costs an addiu per store. Do not re-spell the subscript, and do not re-brief this residual as a hand job.
- id: a-typed-panel-local-pays-the-giv | keywords: addu, operand, order, panel, giv, typed, pointer, local, reload, hedged
  **TYPING A `void *` PANEL BASE AS ITS STRUCT POINTER COSTS THE PER-ITERATION RELOAD: cc1 then keeps `panel + i * stride` in a callee-saved GIV** (round 405, menu/main/misc9 func_801DD5E8, parked 99.72% at retail's exact 424 bytes / 106 insns). Retail reloads `g_Menu->unk430` inside the loop, once before the `if` and once after the call, and re-forms `panel + i * 0x80` from it each time (`lw v1,1072(v0)` then `addu v0,v1,s0`); so the panel base must stay a memory load at every use. Three fully typed spellings measured from the same body, all worse and all for this one reason: `MenuString* pPanel; pPanel = g_Menu->unk430; pString = &pPanel[i];` with subscripts throughout (86.25%, 384 bytes), the same with `pPanel[i + 0xE]` at the second call site (89.75%, 404 bytes, frame 0x30), and `pString[0xE]` off the loop's live entry pointer (96 insns). Each turns the sum into a GIV into `$s0` that survives the call. The two surviving `addu` operand swaps are still the legitimiser (`a-addu-index-first-is-the-legitimiser`) and a third permute.sh run found nothing (1386 iters, base=30 best=30).
- id: b-own-pointer-local-inside-each-arm | keywords: addu, operand, pointer, local, arm, if, cross-jump, sll, arm-duplicate
  **AN OWN POINTER LOCAL INSIDE EACH ARM OF AN `if` STOPS cc1 CSE-ING THE INDEX AND MAKES IT DUPLICATE IT PER ARM, WHICH IS ALSO HOW A 4-BYTE SIZE SHORTFALL CLOSES** (round 396, battle/main/misc18 func_80097D5C, 98.63 -> 98.83 with the byte count going from 3020 to retail's exact 3024 / 752 insns and every branch offset then matching). Retail's `if (characterId == 4)` arm pair had a `nop` in the `bne` slot, the `sll` of `i * 8` in the `j` arm's delay slot, the SAME `sll` again at the head of the second arm, and cross-jumped only the store tail; the one-statement form `((u8 *)g_BattleCurWorkBlock + i * 8)[0x1028] = ...;` in both arms made cc1 hoist a single `sll` into the `bne` delay slot and duplicate the `addu` instead - one instruction SHORT, which objdiff reports as 99.x% but which can never link. Writing `pDst = (u8 *)g_BattleCurWorkBlock + i * 8;` as the first statement of EACH arm gives retail's shape exactly (pointer local per arm, `pDst[0x1028] = ...`), so a function that diffs at 99.x% with an exact instruction count one or two low should be checked for an if/else whose two arms index the same base. Beware the converse, measured in the same function: the same per-store pointer local on a RUN of sibling stores (six byte stores in a row) is worse (98.39 with the 2-statement form, 98.43 with the 3-statement in-place form, 96.04 when one `pDst` is shared by all six, which loses three `lui/lw` loads) because there the arms do not exist to block the cse.
- id: b-strength-reduce-giv-is-always-first | keywords: addu, operand, giv, loop, test, preheader, biv, base-first
  **A `strength_reduce` GIV REGISTER IS ALWAYS `addu`'s FIRST OPERAND, so the loop spelling that reproduces retail's BIV-INIT-LAST preheader group gives up base-first `addu` at every site - the two are exclusive in gcc 2.6.0** (round 397, menu/main/misc11_r2_260 func_801E61B0, parked 97.58% at retail's exact 672 bytes / 168 insns; whole body identical except the pair `sw s2,40(sp) / move s2,zero`, whose position is retail's LAST preheader init). Retail's group is `[giv j*0x50][giv j*4][giv j*0x87C+0xA98][biv offset]`, and a front-end accumulator always emits first, before anything loop.c inserts. Dropping the accumulator and writing every use as `j * MENU_SLOT_PRIM_STRIDE` inline (test `j < 3`, which loop.c turns into the giv test `slti s2,0x1974`) DOES produce that group - `[s3][s5][s2][s4]`, j dies, same 672/168 - but all seven `base + offset` sums then come out `addu v0,s2,v0` where retail has `addu v0,v0,s2` (97.17%). Writing the sum index-first in C (`j * STRIDE + (u32)g_Menu->pPrimBuffer`), the `base - (-(j * STRIDE))` neg trick (84.71%, 676, emits `subu` and moves a latch) and a separate front-end accumulator with the test on `j < 3` (680 bytes, j takes `$s7`: the front-end biv and the died-j are exclusive) all fail. Same verdict as `a-addu-index-first-is-the-legitimiser`, now for a loop giv rather than a subscript: judge a park that has BOTH residuals before spending a lane. Round 426 re-measured this body at 97.58 / 672 / 168 and closed the preset leg for the same function (try_flags.sh: gcc-2.7.2-cdk 73.08, `-G0` 97.58 identical, gcc-2.6.0 `-O3` and gcc-2.7.2 fail to compile the unit's own `.i`), and sharpened why the two halves are one register: retail's `$s2` is BOTH the value the `slti` tests AND the addend at all seven body sites, so any body whose initialiser comes from `loop.c` gives up the front-end pseudo those base-first `addu`s belong to. **ROUND 439 CORRECTION, measured first-hand from the parked body in this tree: the giv route's preheader is NOT retail's group either.** It reads `[hoisted g_MenuSlotResourceIds base][giv j*0x50][giv j*4][biv offset][giv primOffset 0xA98]` (97.17 / 672 / 168, 18 differing lines) - so besides the seven `addu` flips it also has `$s2`/`$s4` swapped, i.e. the offset biv's init is recorded before primOffset's derived giv where retail has it last. The front-end route was re-measured from the same body at 97.58 / 672 / 168 with the single displaced pair, confirming the note is not stale, and `for (j = 0, offset = j; ...)` measures identically (the front end folds `j` to 0, so the init stays in the entry block). Both routes therefore fall short in a different place and neither is one edit from 100%: treat this function's park as closed, not as a 97.58 body waiting for a spill fix. **ROUND 452 CLOSED THE OTHER LEG and named why the giv route loses a WHOLE INSTRUCTION:** offset written as a body-assigned giv with the test `j * MENU_SLOT_PRIM_STRIDE < 0x1974` (loop.c rewrites it back into the giv test `slti s2,6516`, so the loop shape is retail's) measures 93.90% / 668 bytes / 167 insns, and the missing instruction is not a register: once offset is a giv, loop.c's related-giv use rewrites the `func_8002675C` argument `(offset + sizeof(MenuSlotQuads)) + pPrimBuffer` onto the primOffset accumulator, so retail's `addiu v0,s2,0xA98` + `addu v0,v0,t1` collapse into one `addu`. The `addiu` therefore only exists while `offset + 0xA98` is a biv-plus-constant add, i.e. while offset is the front-end biv - the exact opposite of what retail's preheader order needs, so the two legs of this park are provably exclusive rather than one edit apart. Also measured there: the giv emission order is not source-position controlled (moving the primOffset assignment below the inner loop is byte-identical, same 93.90 / 668).



- id: a-u-char-local-width-drives | keywords: regalloc, u-char, andi, mask, local, sweep, declaration, tie
  **A `u_char` LOCAL THAT LOOKS LIKE IT ONLY EXISTS TO MAKE A MASK IS LOAD-BEARING: ITS QI WIDTH IS WHAT TURNS `x * 2` INTO `sll` + `andi 0xFE`, and the same-typed `s32` loop counter spelling costs a callee-saved register and 12 bytes** (round 401, menu/main/misc6 func_801D2D38, parked 99.74% at retail's exact 392 bytes / 98 insns). Retail's first `MenuLoadCharacterNameTexture` takes `sll a1,s0,1 / andi a1,a1,0xFE`; passing the `s32` counter itself (`f(id, j * 2)`, the spelling a reader would write) gives 93.87% / 404 bytes and a THIRD callee-saved counter, so `u_char characterIndex = j;` must stay even though it reads like a cast-only local. The second call's argument is the opposite: `6 + (characterIndex * 2)` stops the strength reduction and is 94.28% / 400, while `6 + (j << 1)` is 99.74 - so a mask on one call's argument and a strength-reduced induction variable on the next are both visible signals, not noise. **Cheap dead-end killer measured on the way**: when a lone register tie survives every spelling, sweep ALL declaration orders of the function's locals mechanically (five locals = 120 permutations, `itertools.permutations` over the declaration block + a `diff_func.sh` run each, about 3 minutes) - it is the cheapest test of the allocno-order hypothesis, and if all 120 score identically the tie is a local-alloc contest between two free registers (here $2, the dead stride offset, vs $4, the dead call argument) and belongs in the park note, not in another lane.
- id: a-twin-port-cannot-fix-a-load-tie | keywords: regalloc, twin, tie, load, base, local-alloc, a1
  **A MATCHED TWIN'S IDENTICAL .s DOES NOT MAKE ITS C TRANSFER WHEN THE RESIDUAL IS A LOCAL-ALLOC TIE ON A DYING BASE LOAD, because the twin's own C only reached that instruction from ITS function's pseudo numbering** (round 402, battle/main/misc23 func_800A9FF0, parked 99.90% at retail's exact 816 bytes / 204 insns). lib_gear's LibGearFreeModel (matched 100.00% under XenoLibrary) shows the same `lw v0,0(s1) / lw a1,4(v0) / beqz a1` pair twice that this function is stuck on, so the `$a1` is producible from C in this exact shape family; but the twin's body ported verbatim into battle scores 98.90 (12 differing lines: the two `$a1` pairs PLUS `sw zero,0(s0)` relocated into the recursion tail's delay slot, which proves the tail's inline-vs-slot-pointer split is retail's own), and the same twin body under -G8 is 98.90 while the unit's split body is 99.90 under both -G0 and -G8 (so the gp flag is not the cause either). The site's spelling provably does not decide the tie: embedded assignment in the test, a pool local in each arm, a Temp8-typed field read, and all six declaration orders of the three locals are byte-identical at 99.90, and a second 3-minute permuter run (916 iterations, base=20 best=20) found nothing. Read this before a twin port: the port pays when the diff is structure (a load order, a rotation, a missing block), and does not when the only residual is a dying-base load tie, which is a whole-function allocator decision.

- id: a-scratch-store-group-swap-is-the-addressing | keywords: regalloc, scratchpad, store, swapped, absolute, array, psx-scratch, twin, li
  **TWO SCRATCHPAD WORD PAIRS THAT COME OUT IN ONE REGISTER ARE FIXED BY SPELLING THE STORES THROUGH A `u32 *pScratch = (u32 *)PSX_SCRATCH` ARRAY, NOT BY ABSOLUTE-ADDRESS CASTS - the instructions are identical, the allocator's picks are not** (round 403, world_map/main/misc4 func_8007A1B4, 97.16 -> 100.00 at retail's exact 604 bytes / 151 insns). Retail stores one value to 0x1F800008/0x1F800000, loads a second, then stores `li 0x1000` to 0x14/0x4 and the second value to 0x18/0x10, holding the first value in `$v0` and the second in `$v1`. One `s32` local reassigned between the two pairs merges the two non-overlapping webs and gives `$v1` for both (99.90 park); two locals with the second load placed BETWEEN the first pair and the constant pair - the shape misc5's matched WorldMapUpdateGroup4And5Scale uses - is not enough on its own: with `*(u32 *)0x1F800008 = angle;` absolute casts it still scores 97.16 and hands the second value `$a2` (`lw a2,112(a2)`, p's dying register) and the first `$v1`. Changing ONLY the store spelling to the twin's `pScratch[2] = angle; pScratch[0] = angle; scale = p->scale; pScratch[5] = 0x1000; pScratch[1] = 0x1000; pScratch[6] = scale; pScratch[4] = scale;` closes it, because six separate address pseudos that each die at their store are a different local-alloc contest from one `reg/v` base plus offsets (both still assemble to `lui $at` + `sw off($at)` under this unit's maspsx). The twin's port paid twice here: the same body also gives retail's 3+3+2 then 4+4 matrix block moves via `*(MATRIX *)&pScratch[0x3C] = g_WorldMapIdentityMatrix;` and `*pMtxB = *pMtxA;`.

- id: a-dead-store-to-an-unused-local | keywords: regalloc, dead-store, local, priority, callee-saved, in-place-or
  **A DEAD STORE TO A NEVER-READ LOCAL IS LOAD-BEARING IN THE ALLOCATOR'S PRIORITY CONTEST - deleting it can cost a callee-saved register, so a body's unused-looking variable may be exactly what keeps the frame retail's size** (round 403, battling/main/misc6 func_8007E020, parked 99.78% at retail's exact 556 bytes / 138 insns). The body's only residual is the OT-tag OR, three times: retail `or a0,a0,t7 / sw a0,0(v1)` (in place on the dying loaded link word) against the compile's `or v0,a0,t7 / sw v0,0(v1)`. Removing the one dead `masked = (u32)&pQuad[1] & 0xFFFFFF;` and its declaration drops the body to 93.19% at 568 bytes, because an extra `sw s1,4(sp)`/`lw s1,4(sp)` pair appears: the dead store's pseudo is what keeps the loop's 9 t registers + one s register at retail's count, so leave it and say so in the note. The related trade is measured on the same body: making the OR in place (`link |= 0x3000000; *pTag = link;`, shared variable) DOES give retail's `or a0,a0,t7` (93.51%, 568 bytes) but rotates the whole prologue by one t register (pLink's address moves $v0 -> $a1, viewX/viewY spill to s1+s0), and per-group `pLink0..2`/`pTag0..2`/`link0..2` instead makes ONE of the three groups exact (98.63%, 556 bytes, groups 1/3 pair-swapped) - plain or in-place OR compiles identically there. Preset screen on the same body: 2.7.2-cdk 94.48, 2.6.0 99.50, Default 99.78; third permuter run base 30 best 30. So on a 3x-repeated read-modify-write whose only residual is the result register, try (i) keep every dead local, (ii) in-place compound assignment, (iii) per-group variables, and read which of the repeated sites came out exact before parking.
- id: a-block-local-entry-load-goes-to-v0 | keywords: regalloc, entry, block-local, lbu, beqz, local-alloc, global-alloc, tie
  **A BYTE LOAD THAT FEEDS THE FUNCTION'S FIRST BRANCH IS ALLOCATED `$v0` BY gcc-2.6.0's local-alloc, so retail's `lbu $a1` for the same two instructions means that value's live range SPANNED BLOCKS and global-alloc coloured it** (round 405, battle/main/misc1 func_80071964, parked 99.51% at retail's exact 164 bytes / 41 insns under XenoBattle260: 40 of 41 instructions identical, the residual is the entry `lbu a1,D_800D2CAF` against our `lbu v0`). The `-dl` dump settles it in one compile: the flag is a block-local qty (`Register 73 used 2 times across 2 insns in block 0`, allocated `2`), while the only globally allocated pseudo in the function (`72 ... crosses 4 calls`) took `$s1` - and the block-spanning entry loads that DO land in `$a1` elsewhere in the repo (libarchive's `ArchiveReleaseStreamBlock`/`ArchiveReleaseStreamSection`, `lui a1/lw a1` then `beqz a1` then a body use) are exactly that case. Repo-wide the shape is common and lands in `$v0`: 130 matched functions whose first two instructions are a `lui`+load pair feeding the first branch put the value in `$v0` (only 5 of the 130 name anything else). Twenty spellings of this body all emit `lbu $2`: compound `&&`, plain, `!()`, nested ifs, two ifs, early return, a `goto` label, `if (0)`, `while (0)`, `switch (D_800D2CAF)`, `u8`/`char`/`int`/`register`/initialiser locals, a pointer deref, `volatile`, `D_800D2CAE[1]`, a hoisted `u16` mask temp, and `-fschedule-insns`/`2`; `flag == 1` does reach `$3` but costs `li`+`bne` (42 insns). Preset screened on the same body: unit flags 99.51, `-G0` 99.51, gcc-2.7.2 98.78 (and 2.7.2 drops four matched neighbours of the unit), cdk 94.59, 2.6.0 `-O1` 57.05, `-O3` 69.02 - so do not move the unit. The criterion for the next agent: only a spelling that keeps the load and its test in DIFFERENT basic blocks can move this register, and every such spelling measured here adds an instruction, so park on the first measurement instead of permuting. Round 418 measured the census that closes it instead of guessing: of the 171 matched functions whose first two insns are `lui r,%hi(sym)` plus a load into that same `r` feeding the entry branch, 155 take `$v0` and every non-`$v0` one is either a parameter or a value read again in a LATER block (libarchive's `ArchiveReleaseStreamBlock`/`ArchiveReleaseStreamSection`, the only two `$a1`), so a dying byte load has no `$a1` precedent anywhere in this repo; a third permuter run (1351 iterations, base 20 best 20) found nothing. Scan recipe: walk `asm/*/matchings/**/*.s`, parse `/* addr addr word */ op operands`, and keep the files whose insn 0 is `lui R` with insn 1 a load into `R` and a branch on `R` within the next six.

- id: a-printf-argument-delta-must-be | keywords: regalloc, printf, argument, staged, local, tie, delay-slot, volatile
  **AN ARITHMETIC `printf` ARGUMENT HAS TO BE STAGED THROUGH A LOCAL IN TWO STATEMENTS (`q = tail; q = q - head;`) OR THE WHOLE ARGUMENT LIST RE-ALLOCATES: the inline `(tail - head) & 0x3F` costs 5% even though the instruction multiset is nearly the same** (round 405, slus_006.64/psyq/libgpu_c func_80046F30, parked 99.51% at retail's exact 364 bytes / 91 insns, PSY-Q preset -O3 -G0). Retail computes the queue delta in `$a1` (`lw a1,g_GpuQueueTail / lw t0,g_GpuQueueHead / subu a1,a1,t0`) and masks it in the `jal` delay slot; `queued = g_GpuQueueTail; queued = queued - g_GpuQueueHead;` reproduced that AND left only two registers of the five-argument list wrong, while `printf(fmt, (g_GpuQueueTail - g_GpuQueueHead) & 0x3F, ...)` measures 94.49 and one statement with the head in a local (`head = g_GpuQueueHead; printf(..., (g_GpuQueueTail - head) & 0x3F, ...)`) 94.38 - both re-order the argument setup, so measure the two-statement form before concluding the residual is an allocator tie. The surviving tie is caller-saved and `-dl`-provable: the dump's per-pseudo lines name the values (`Register 81 used 3 times across 22 insns` = the `g_GpuGp1Port` pointer load, disposed to `$6`/`$a2` where retail has `$3`/`$v1`; `Register 83 used 2 times across 3 insns` = the `g_GpuQueueHead` value, `$3` where retail has `$8`), and both ref counts are pinned by the byte stream (each value is read exactly twice), which is the same no-spelling-moves-it verdict as the callee-saved `-dg` levers - park after one look. Also measured on that body: storing the deliberately discarded volatile read to an unused local changes nothing (99.51), gcc-2.7.2-cdk 65.03, and a fifth permuter run (1790 iterations, base=45 best=45, NONE). Recipe for a psyq (-O3 -G0) unit: `docker run --rm -v"$PWD":/w -w /w --platform=linux/amd64 ethos:latest` with `tools/gcc-2.7.2-psx/cc1` + `cc_flags` + `-O3 -G0 -dl -dg` on `build/src/<unit>.c.i`; the dumps land beside it as `<input>.lreg` / `<input>.greg`.

- id: a-shared-counter-forces-the-callee-saved-register | keywords: regalloc, counter, shared, callee-saved, calls-crossed, reuse, loop, xeno
  **WHEN ONE LOOP COUNTER SITS IN A CALLEE-SAVED REGISTER AND THAT LOOP HAS NO CALL, THE VARIABLE IS SHARED WITH A LATER LOOP THAT DOES: give the free-pass counter and the next nest's counter ONE variable and the whole hand-out can fall in** (round 430, world_map/main/misc16 func_80098CC0, 97.03 -> 97.46% at retail's exact 1644 bytes / 411 insns, XenoWorldMapDiv, 192 -> 138 differing lines; the free pass and all four nest bodies then match instruction for instruction). Retail kept the free pass's search counter `j` in `$s4` while that loop contains no call, and its nest outer counter was `$s4` too, which is only possible for a single pseudo whose other live ranges span the nest's `HeapAlloc`/`WorldMapAddCdRequest` calls - `global.c` withholds the caller-saved class from any allocno that crosses a call, so a plain two-variable body hands `j` to `$a0` and drags the prologue's base pointers with it. The edit is pure source: delete the nest counter's declaration and reuse the free-pass counter in all four `for (c = 1; c != -1; c--)` heads. It COSTS nothing when it is wrong (identical bytes), so it belongs in the first three probes for any "free-pass counter in an s-reg with no call in sight" park. The residual it leaves is the tile-id index register (`$s0` retail, `$v0` ours in all eight `li`/`sll` pairs) plus the two 4-iteration corner loops; `grid[idx++]` (413 insns / wrong size, 95.92), `u32 idx` and a separate stride-9 index variable (both byte-identical) are measured dead. Round 443 took the dump on the same body and it closes that axis: `-da`/`-dg` name the walk index as pseudo 78, `20 refs across 24 insns; dies in 4 places`, NO call crossed, with `78 preferences: 2` - the only preferred allocno in the second half - so it is coloured `$v0` in all eight `li`/`sll` pairs, while retail's three-instruction-live value sits in `$s0`, i.e. retail's allocno spanned a call and no spelling of a dead constant reaches that without changing the instruction count. The same dump shows the register file is retail's exactly (40 allocnos, hard regs 2-8 and 16-23 plus 30, one `$t0` spill), so the surviving corner-loop difference is a pure 4-cycle of saved registers and the next handle is whatever gives pseudo 78 its `$v0` preference, not the allocator order.

- id: a-one-counter-per-loop-nest-moves | keywords: regalloc, counter, loop, reused, variable, split, callee-saved, rotation
  **A LOOP COUNTER'S REGISTER IS PER SOURCE VARIABLE, SO REUSING ONE LOCAL ACROSS A FREE PASS AND A LATER NEST PINS BOTH LOOPS TO THE SAME REGISTER AND ROTATES THE WHOLE FUNCTION - splitting the nest's counters into their own variables moved eight diff lines for free** (round 405, world_map/main/misc16 func_80098CC0, 96.96 -> 97.03 at retail's exact 1644 bytes / 411 insns, XenoWorldMapDiv). Retail is NOT consistent per variable: its free-pass counters are `$s2`/`$s4` while the nest loops use `$s4`/`$s2` (swapped), which is only possible if the two loop pairs were distinct C variables. With one `i`/`j` shared by all four loops the free-pass outer counter lands in `$s5` where retail has `$s2`; giving the nest loops their own `group`/`cell` puts that counter in `$s2` (retail's) - measured 97.03 against 96.96 for the shared body, 96.90 for sharing only the outer counter and 96.95 for sharing only the inner one, and declaration order of the new locals changes nothing (identical code). The residual that survives on that function is an allocator rotation between the nest counter (`group`, ours `$s5` / retail `$s4`) and the sector pointer (`pSector`, ours `$s4` / retail `$s5`) plus retail's tile-id index constant in `$s0` where the compile uses `$v0`. Two things measured on the way that are worth knowing: assigning the index before the `ArchiveDecodeSector` call, i.e. making it live ACROSS the call, does force a callee-saved register but SHRINKS the body to 409 insns / 1636 bytes, so an exact-size park proves that is not the original; and a separate id variable for a 4-iteration corner loop costs 3 insns and 92.86%. `try_flags.sh` on the same body: cdk 85.85, -O3 96.96, -G0 and 2.6.0 do not compile, so the unit's preset is right. SUPERSEDED IN PART (round 430): the two shared-counter probes above shared the free pass's counter with the SAME loop role in the nest (outer with outer, inner with inner); sharing the free-pass INNER counter with the nest OUTER counter, i.e. making one pseudo whose later ranges cross the nest's calls, is worth 97.46% and 138 differing lines - see `a-shared-counter-forces-the-callee-saved-register` before re-parking this function.

- id: a-call-argument-constant-add-never-ties | keywords: regalloc, in-place, addu, addiu, argument, pointer, tie, probe
  **A CALL ARGUMENT THAT IS `pointer + const` NEVER TIES IN PLACE: cc1 2.6.0 AND 2.7.2 BOTH COMPUTE IT INTO THE ARGUMENT REGISTER FROM A SEPARATE BASE (`addu $4,$2,400`, which maspsx prints as `addiu a0,v0,400`), SO RETAIL'S `addiu a0,a0,const` BEFORE A `jal` IS NOT REACHABLE FROM `(u8 *)p + n`, `p += n` OR A COPY OF IT** (round 409, battle/main/misc2 func_800745EC, parked 99.87% at retail's EXACT 1228 bytes / 0x4CC / 307 insns with the whole body instruction-identical except this one register and the unit-owned switch table). `cc1_probe.sh battle/main/misc2` on `p = gx; count = p[0x66E]; start = p[0x66A]; p += 0x190; BattleAddPrimListToOT(p, count, start);` emits `lbu $5,1646($2) / lbu $6,1642($2) / addu $4,$2,400` - an in-place C update still gets a FRESH output pseudo because the call's copy suggestion pins that pseudo to `$4` and the loaded base keeps `$2`. Measured on the real body, each at 99.87% / 1228 bytes: the inline `(u8 *)p + 0x190` (base `$v1`), a separate `u8 *pPrims = (u8 *)p` copy (`$v1`), and an in-place `pPrims += 0x190` with the two bytes pre-loaded through `pPrims` (`$v0`). try_flags on the same body: gcc-2.7.2 99.87, gcc-2.7.2-cdk 90.63, so the preset is not the cause. The permuter cannot run on it at all - its parser rejects `(u8 *)` argument casts with "conversion to non-scalar type requested" and the base does not compile. Park this shape; do not spend a lane on it.

- id: b-palette-store-pointer-inline-pool-load | keywords: load, order, store, pointer, pool, field, pbase, palette
  **A POINTER LOCAL ASSIGNED FROM A GLOBAL'S POINTER FIELD LOADS THAT FIELD BEFORE THE INDEX MULTIPLY; FOLDING THE POOL LOAD INTO THE POINTER'S OWN STATEMENT REPRODUCES RETAIL'S INTERLEAVED ORDER** (round 413, battle/main/misc16_r2 func_800904A0, 79.85 -> 91.97, 624 bytes / 156 insns against retail's 620 / 155, parked). Retail's palette store block reads `lw v1,0(s1)` (the prim index), `sll/addu/sll` by 0x28, THEN `lui+lw g_BattleGraphicsWork`, `lw pBase,0xA230(...)`, `lbu D_800D2F69[row*4]`, `beqz`, `addu` in the delay slot. `pBase = g_BattleGraphicsWork->pPacketPrims;` followed by `pRec = pBase + *pMode * 0x28;` loads the pool first and finishes the multiply last (79.85); assigning the same expression inline in the pointer's own statement, `pRec = g_BattleGraphicsWork->pPacketPrims + *pMode * 0x28;`, gives retail's order in both halves at +12 points. The neighbouring call in the same function wants the two-statement form (retail loads the pool before the index there), so the two spellings coexist in one function - check the load order of each block separately before declaring the whole body one shape. Inlining the store instead (`*(u16 *)(pool + (*pMode * 0x28 + 0x32E)) = pal;`) is worse, 78.32, because the store then lands after the palette if/else.
- id: a-local-base-sp-vs-live-pointer-tie | keywords: regalloc, sp, fp, base, stack, local, address, tie, mirror
  **A LOCAL'S ADDRESS HAS TWO EQUAL ENCODINGS (`sp+offset` and the live `&local` register) AND cc1 PICKS ONE PER USE, so a body whose two remaining lines are the exact MIRROR of retail's (`addiu a0,sp,0x28` + `sb v0,0x10(s8)` against `addiu a0,s8,8` + `sb v0,0x30(sp)`) is an allocation tie you close by REUSING AN EXISTING INDEX EXPRESSION, not by a pointer local** (round 417, shop_menu/main/misc func_801D05BC, 99.97 -> 100.00 at retail's exact 1628 bytes / 407 insns, gcc 2.6.0). The body writes five glyph codes through `((u8*)&rect)[8 + k * 2]` in a `for (j = 4, k = 0; j > 0; k++, j--)` loop and then the units digit at `((u8*)&rect)[16]`: spelling that last store as the loop's OWN expression with the POST-LOOP k, `((u8*)&rect)[8 + k * 2]` (k == 4, so 16), reproduced retail's register-based `sb v0,16(s8)` and kept the `li s2,0x28` and `li t0,0xC3` hoists, while the literal `16` kept the sp base and also flipped s7/s8. The decode argument then had to be the separate local (`SystemDecodeGlyphCodesN((u16*)digitBuffer, ...)`, sp+0x28), not `((u8*)&rect + 8)`. Measured and worse on the same body: a `RECT* pRect = &rect` at the loop top (or before the digit loop, or a `u8*` pointer) 97.68 or 99.41 with 1632 bytes - a live digit pointer costs the `li s2,0x28` hoist and moves `i` from s3 to s2; `((u8*)&rect)[new_var * 2]` 99.10; a `u16*` temp for the decode arg changes nothing (99.97, folded); the twin's own shape ported wholesale (`u8 digitGlyphs[0xE]` + live pRect for the stores) 97.83/1632 even with the old-style extern; the honest `digitBuffer[k*2]` / `digitBuffer[8]` spelling 95.94/1616. Three earlier permuter runs plus this round's 3-minute run (869 iterations, base 5 best 5) found nothing, so the permuter does not cross this class - count how many registers retail's hoisted constants occupy and keep the digit buffer addressed through `&rect` plus a shared index expression.

  CONFIRMED on the same unit's buy twin func_801CDD14 (round 435, 97.80 -> 100.00 at retail's exact
  1900 bytes / 475 insns, gcc 2.6.0): dropping the `RECT* pRect = &rect;` local and spelling the
  units digit as `((u8*)&rect)[8 + k * 2]` (post-loop k) plus `LoadImage(&rect, ...)` restored the
  `li s2,0x28` hoist and moved i from `$s2` back to `$s3` - the whole register cascade in one edit.
  That park had a full note of measured negatives (literal 0x1000, u16 polyColor, s16 rectWidth,
  u8 blankDigit, three presets) and none of them touched the pointer local, which was the cause.

- id: a-in-place-chain-on-a-named-local | keywords: regalloc, in-place, read-modify-write, chain, local, mask, li, load
  **WHEN RETAIL DOES A FLAG-WORD READ-MODIFY-WRITE IN ONE REGISTER (`load / and mask / or value / store`, all on the same register) WITH THE MASK CONSTANT IN ANOTHER, THE CHAIN MUST BE WRITTEN AS IN-PLACE COMPOUND STATEMENTS ON A NAMED LOCAL - `flags = p0->flags; flags &= ~MASK; flags |= X; p0->flags = flags;` - because the one-expression form gives the `and` a FRESH destination in the mask's dying register** (round 417, battling/main/misc2 func_80074BA4, 99.74 -> 100.00 at retail's exact 1212 bytes / 303 insns, Default preset; four `switch` arms each had the same residual). Retail per arm: `addiu v1,zero,-0x801; ... lw a0,0xD0(s0); and a0,a0,v1; or a0,a0,v0; sw a0,0xD0(s0)` - mask in `$v1`, loaded word and the whole chain in `$a0`. Measured, all 303 insns / 1212 bytes: the one-expression field form `p0->flags = (p0->flags & ~0x800) | ((ret & 1) << 11);` mirrors BOTH the mask and the word (`li a0,-2049 / lw v1` then `and v1,v1,a0`) in the two arms whose stored value is not re-tested afterwards, and is exact in the two that are - 99.74; `flags = p0->flags; p0->flags = (flags & ~0x800) | ((ret & 1) << 11);` puts the load and the mask right and the `and`'s destination in `$v1` - 99.87; the same with the chain on the local and one store at the end is 100.00 in all four arms. So the load has to be its OWN statement (that pins the word's register first) and the updates have to be `&=`/`|=` on that same local (one pseudo, no fresh destination for combine to allocate). Note this is the mirror image of `a-chain-and-a-scratch-need-two` (battling/main/misc4 func_80079DF0), where the same in-place form scored 99.89 and the one-expression form 99.96: which of the two wins depends on whether retail's chain shares one register, so try both spellings before parking a flag-word RMW.

- id: a-a-countdown-counter-reusing-the | keywords: regalloc, callee-saved, counter, loop, rotation, frame, reuse, move
  **A COUNTDOWN LOOP WHOSE COUNTER RETAIL DECREMENTS IN PLACE ON A VALUE THAT WAS LIVE ACROSS A CALL MEANS THE SOURCE REUSED THAT VARIABLE AS THE COUNTER - a separate `i = rest - 1` local takes a caller-saved register and rotates EVERY callee-saved value and the frame** (round 419, slus_006.64/psyq/libgpu_b func_800460A0, 86.60 -> 94.79 on the register fix alone, then 98.36 at 568 of retail's 572 bytes, PSY-Q preset). Retail's LoadImage keeps rect/p/blocks/the 0x04000000 mask in $s1/$s2/$s4/$s3 and the CPU-sent tail counter in $s0, decremented in place (`addiu s0,s0,-1` both in the loop and in the preheader, test `beq/bne s0,-1`); with `for (i = rest - 1; i != -1; i--)` the counter is created after the DMA-wait loop, needs no callee-saved register, lands in $a0 and rect/p/blocks/mask each drop one register ($s0/$s1/$s3/$s2) with a 0x58 frame instead of 0x50. Writing `while (--rest != -1) *g_GpuGp0Port = *p++;` - the SAME variable that held `total/2 - blocks*16`, which is live across the `jal` in the wait loop - reproduces retail's $s0 and every other register with no other change. Same round, same function: the 2 instructions and 4 bytes were `blocks = total / 32`, whose signed division rounding (`addiu x,32 / sra`) retail does not have - its plain `sra` says the source shifted (`total >> 5`); and the image command is a conditional expression at the store (`*g_GpuPort = mode ? 0xB0000000 : 0xA0000000;`), not a `cmd` local assigned in an if, which is what puts the constant in the loop latch's delay slot with the `beqz` AFTER the port reload.

- id: a-tail-value-needs-its-own-short-lived-local | keywords: regalloc, rotation, local, live-range, priority, reuse, tail, dl
  **RECYCLING A FUNCTION-WIDE LOCAL FOR A SINGLE BLOCK'S VALUE MAKES IT ONE LONG-LIVED GLOBAL ALLOCNO AND LOSES THE REGISTER CONTEST; GIVING THAT BLOCK ITS OWN LOCAL DROPS ITS LIVE RANGE AND HANDED BACK RETAIL'S WHOLE THREE-REGISTER TAIL IN ONE EDIT** (round 429, world_map/main/misc6_b func_80080600, 99.69 -> 100.00 at retail's exact 768 bytes / 192 insns, XenoWorldMapDiv). The body is instruction-identical except the last five statements, where retail is `mfhi a1` (the rand value) / `lui a3,%hi(D_8009BD42)` (the array base) / `lhu a2,0xA2(s1)` (the scratch reload) and the compile emitted a cyclic rotation `a3/a2/a1`. Three earlier rounds had settled for "global allocation's tie-break over an identical instruction stream" because the `-da` dump listed `$v0 $v1 $a0 $a1 $a2` as conflicting for that pseudo. The value was assigned to `nValue`, a local written in THREE earlier arms (both switch bodies), so one allocno spanned most of the function and its refs/live quotient sank below the two block-local tail pseudos; declaring a fresh `s32 nRand;` used only in the tail raised its priority enough to take `$a1` first and the other two fell into `$a3`/`$a2` on their own (no byte change, no frame change). Two 3-4 minute permuter runs on the shared-local body (base 60 best 60) and two rounds of hand spellings found nothing, so on a 99%+ park whose ONLY residual is a rotation among 2-4 registers in the last basic block, split the shared local per block FIRST - it is free to measure and the same idiom as a-one-arm-local-spanning-a-call in the opposite direction. Companion measurement on this body: retyping the two struct fields the arm writes from `u16 *` to plain `s32` (to drop the `(void *)` casts) forces reloads everywhere and gives 92.39% / 780 bytes, so the `*(volatile s32 *)&pActor->field` spelling is the load-bearing one - do not "clean" it.
- id: a-global-alloc-refs-live-decides-a-loop-pair | keywords: regalloc, dg, dispositions, global-alloc, refs, live, proof, loop
  **TWO VALUES THAT BOTH SPAN A WHOLE LOOP ARE GLOBAL ALLOCNOS, SO global-alloc's refs/live QUOTIENT - NOT -dl's per-block insn counts - ORDERS THEM, and when the instruction stream is already retail's those two counts are pinned, which turns a mirrored caller-saved pair into a proof instead of a tie** (round 433, slus_006.64/system/sound func_8003E900, parked 98.51% at retail's exact 604 bytes / 151 insns, XenoLibrary preset, the whole body instruction-identical with the SPU voice base `$t1` and the 24-iteration counter `$t0` mirrored). Read the pair's numbers off `-dl` (`Register 72 used 8 times across 96 insns; dies in 0 places`) and the assignment off `-dg` (`;; 12 regs to allocate: 73 80 203 75 71 81 72 79 74 78 77 76` plus `;; Register dispositions: 71 in 3  72 in 9  73 in 5 ...`, where 2/3 = $v0/$v1 and 8/9 = $t0/$t1): pVoice 8 refs / 96 insns = 0.083 loses to the counter 13 refs / 95 = 0.137. Every ref is a mention in an insn retail also has - the base only in the preheader def, a derived-address giv, one `sh 0(t0)` and its `addiu`; the counter in three `sllv`, its `addiu` and the `slti` - so a flip needs the base at 14 refs or the counter at 8, i.e. two instructions that are not in retail's stream. Recipe in one container run: `cp build/src/<unit>.c.i /tmp/m.i && tools/<gcc>/cc1 <the build.ninja cc_flags> -O2 -G8 -dl -dg -o /tmp/m.s /tmp/m.i`, then awk the `;; Function <name>` section out of `/tmp/m.i.lreg` and `.greg`. Related, opposite direction: `a-tail-value-needs-its-own-short-lived-local`, where SPLITTING a local per block moved the quotient the right way. Also measured dead on this body: a u_char counter (adds the widening `andi`, 96.36 / 608 bytes), `-G0` (same 98.51), gcc-2.7.2 (98.51) and gcc-2.7.2-cdk (98.31).

- id: a-sched1-hoists-a-ternary-constant | keywords: regalloc, sched, sched1, conflict, interval, dg, ternary, caller-saved
  **A TERNARY-ARM CONSTANT IN THE WRONG CALLER-SAVED REGISTER IS USUALLY sched1's FAKE LIVE-RANGE, NOT A SPELLING: `-da`/`-dg` shows the constant's HI pseudo conflicting with the register retail gives it, because sched1 hoisted the `movhi` of the constant to the HEAD of the block (scheduler priority 1 - its only reader is the store in the NEXT block), so check `m.i.sched` before permuting** (round 433, battling/main/misc8 func_80081A44, parked 99.70% at retail's exact 744 bytes / 186 insns, Default preset; whole body instruction-identical except retail `li v0,5 / li v0,4 / sh v0,D_800916E2` against the compile's `$a0` - culprit.sh reports 3 bytes, 0x80081AEA-0x80081AF6). At combine time the block is `[94 li v1,1][96 sw latched][101 lw flags][102 andi][502 li 5][104 bnez]`; `m.i.sched` prints `;; 104 (7fffff66)` against `ready list at T-3: 502 (1)`, and the post-sched1 order is `[502][101][94][96][102][104]`, so the HI pseudo is live across the `lw` and `m.i.greg` then says `84 conflicts: 84 2 3 29` with `84 in 4` - $v0 is barred by the load that retail puts in it. Retail's sched1 left 502 in the delay slot, and no C spelling moves it: measured dead on this body at 744 bytes are `(x & 1) ? 5 : 4` arm order (99.37), a `(u16)` cast on the ternary (99.70, identical), one `s32 flags` reused as the ternary result to force the load and the arms into one pseudo (748, 98.90, the `andi` then reads $v1), moving the latched store after the ternary (96.91), plus the whole 744-byte recipe the park note holds (volatile first store + volatile second read as a statement). Preset screens on the same body: gcc-2.7.2-cdk 99.70, -O3 99.70, -O1 81.79, -Os and 2.6.0 reject it. Two 3-minute permuter runs report base=45 best=45 (the score is dominated by branch-label renaming, so it cannot see a one-register move). So: on a one-register caller-saved residual where retail's register is one your compile already used just above, read `-da`/`-dg`; a conflict created by a hoisted constant is a park, not a spelling hunt. Recipe: `cp build/src/<unit>.c.i build/.dump/m.i && docker run --rm -i --platform=linux/amd64 -v"$PWD":/w -w /w ethos:latest tools/gcc-2.7.2-psx/cc1 <cc_flags> -O2 -G8 -da -o m.s m.i` (docker `-v` only works under the repo path), then read `m.i.greg`/`m.i.lreg`/`m.i.sched` for the `;; Function <name>` section.

- id: a-third-allocno-holds-the-contested-register | keywords: regalloc, swapped, tie, dg, dispositions, third, constant, park
  **A MIRRORED CALLEE-SAVED PAIR IS OFTEN DECIDED BY A THIRD, SHORT-LIVED ALLOCNO THAT GRABBED THE LOWER REGISTER FIRST - read the `-dg` DISPOSITIONS list for it before concluding the two sides' refs/live quotients are the whole story** (round 434, battling/main/misc10 func_800852C4, re-parked 99.24% at retail's exact 2928 bytes / 732 insns, Default preset, whole body instruction-identical with pScene `$s3`/retail `$s2` and the const-1/`~1`/`&g_BattleMatchCount` chain `$s2`/retail `$s3`). The two candidates look like a plain quotient contest and are not: pseudo 72 = pScene "used 15 times across 463 insns" (0.39), pseudo 75 = pMatchCount "7 times across 240 insns" (0.23), but pseudo 122 = the `const_int 1` feeding the `g_BattleTransitionFlag == 1` bne "4 times across 58 insns" (0.55) is processed between them and takes `$18`, so pScene is pushed to `$19` and pMatchCount later takes the `$18` 122 released - which is retail's arrangement exactly, mirrored. Nothing in the source moves 122's quotient (its four refs are the def, the branch, the `= 1` store and the REG_EQUAL), so the function parks; the one handle that has flipped a pair in this unit is a variable split that changes the total allocno set (splitting the audio-manager block out of the pBuf variable, in that function's park note, moved a different pair and bought 99.19 -> 99.24). Diagnostic: dump with `-da`/`-dg`, list the dispositions of the contested registers (`122 in 18  72 in 19  75 in 18`) and the processing order, and name every allocno that sits in the register before the mirrored one's turn - if that allocno is a constant or a short-lived pointer, no spelling of either mirrored value reaches the pair. Three permuter runs on this function (10 min 3929 -> 665, 7 min 665 -> 580, 3 min base 620 best 535 where the winner merged `state` into `idleTimer`, wrong code). Round 447 closed the last two axes on the same body and turned the verdict into arithmetic: the preset screen leaves nothing (gcc-2.6.0 99.24, i.e. the IDENTICAL mirrored allocation, so the pair is not a compiler-version question; cdk 91.22; plain -G0 99.24; -O3 and cdk -G0 do not compile), and the -da numbers fit `floor_log2(refs) * refs * size / live_length` exactly, so the quotient gaps can be read as margins: pScene 72 is 3*15*4/463 = 0.389 against 122's 2*4*4/58 = 0.552, and 72 only overtakes it at refs >= 16 (floor_log2 jumps to 4) or at live_length <= 326 insns. pScene is created at the top of the 732-insn function and last used in the case-5 arm, so one more reference to it is one instruction more than retail's 2928 bytes - a 15-ref pseudo sitting exactly on a floor_log2 step is the cheapest way to see whether the flip is one ref away. A fourth permuter run was skipped on that arithmetic, not on the three earlier ones.

- id: a-source-pointer-walk-is-the-longest-lived-biv | keywords: regalloc, biv, pointer, live, dg, subscript, strength-reduce, loop
  **A LOOP-CARRIED POINTER WALK (`pValue = g_Table; ... pValue += 8`) IS INITIALISED BEFORE THE LOOP, SO ITS ALLOCNO HAS THE LONGEST LIVE RANGE OF THE LOOP'S BIVS AND GLOBAL-ALLOC HANDS IT THE LAST OF `$t5`-`$t7`; WRITING THE SAME ACCESS AS AN 8-BYTE-RECORD SUBSCRIPT (`g_Table[slot].field`) MAKES loop.c STRENGTH-REDUCE IT INTO A PREHEADER BIV LIKE RETAIL'S AND FLIPS THE WHOLE ROTATION AT NO BYTE COST** (round 435, battle/main/misc2 func_80075168, 98.87 -> 100.00 at retail's exact 2000 bytes / 500 insns, XenoLibrary = gcc-2.6.0; a park that had been re-measured four times under "allocation only"). The park's 58 differing lines were one 3-cycle: retail `dv=$t5, strided-index=$t6, 0x28-biv=$t7` against ours `$t7/$t5/$t6`, plus the two hoisted `0x22`/`0x26` constants mirrored in `$s0`/`$s1` and the `lui/addiu` of the table address hoisted to the top of the function instead of 5th in the preheader. The `-da`/`-dg` numbers explain all of it at once: the three bivs are 9 refs each (all SI), so `floor_log2(9)*9/live` ranks them purely by live_length, and the pointer's 399 vs 393/391 is ITS OWN SOURCE STATEMENT, evaluated before the preheader, being its definition - hence `$t7`, the last register handed out. Retail's pointer init sits 5th among six preheader inits because it is a biv loop.c created, not a statement the programmer wrote. One edit closes it (the declaration becomes `typedef struct { u8 value; u8 unk1[7]; } BattleSlotDisplayValue;` + `g_BattleSlotDisplayValue[slot].value * 2`), and the same edit fixed the mirrored constants and the prologue order for free, which is the general lesson: when a park's residual is several registers at once, look for the pseudo whose LIVE RANGE ORIGIN (not its count) differs, not for each mirrored register separately. Companion measurement on the same body: the last instruction, retail's base-first `addu v0,a3,t8` for `pGraphics[slot * 0x1E4 + 0x853D]`, wants the sum in a pointer local (`pSlotGraphics = pGraphics + slot * 0x1E4; pSlotGraphics[0x853D]`, the misc11_b func_800826CC lever) while KEEPING `pGraphics` as a local for the 28 prim stores - dropping that local instead (both sums off `g_BattleGraphicsWork`) reloads the global per store and costs 3 instructions / 2012 bytes / 98.63, and a front-end `off3 += 0x1E4` accumulator moves `move t8,zero` to the top of the function (99.45).
- id: a-call-arg-pool-reads-as-one-shot-expressions | keywords: regalloc, argument, a0, cse, one-shot, local, call, pool
  **A CALL WHOSE FIRST ARGUMENT IS `pool + K` AND WHOSE OTHER ARGUMENTS ARE FIELDS OF THAT SAME POOL PUTS THE POOL IN `$a0` ONLY WHEN ALL THREE USES ARE ONE-SHOT CSE'D EXPRESSIONS - a named `p` local loads it into `$v1` and costs a register, 0.07% and a `MATCH`** (round 436, battle/main/misc2 func_800745EC, 99.90 -> 99.97% at retail's exact 1228 bytes / 307 insns, XenoLibrary = gcc-2.6.0, the switch's own jtbl operand the only residual, `check_bin.sh battle` OK). Retail is `lw a0,-0x5DD0(at) / nop / lbu a1,0x66E(a0) / lbu a2,0x66A(a0) / jal / addiu a0,a0,0x190`, i.e. the pool load lands in the ARGUMENT register and the `+K` reuses it in the delay slot; with `p = ((Work *)g_Work)->pPacketPrims; BattleAddPrimListToOT((u8 *)p + 0x190, p->digitCount, p->pairPrimIndex)` the load goes to `$v1` and the arg becomes `addiu a0,v1,0x190` - same instruction count, same bytes. Spelling all three uses as the full `((BattlePacketPrims *)((Work *)g_Work)->pPacketPrims)->field` expression (cse merges them into one load) gives retail's `$a0` for free. This is `a-inline-field-reads-beat-locals` at a CALL site rather than a two-field difference, and the same function's earlier gain was the same lever on a `labelDrawn` test, so on a one-register residual at a call whose args all come off one loaded pointer, replace the pointer local with the inline expression at every use before permuting. Measured dead on the same call site at 99.90 / 1228: a `u8 *pDest` local, a second `BattlePacketPrims *q` local, `(u8 *)&p->prims[10]`, reading the two bytes before forming the pointer, and a three-copies-of-the-field-expression form that keeps `p` for the argument - and the permuter cannot run on the body at all (its randomizer rewrites the `(u8 *)` argument casts into a non-scalar cast and gcc rejects base.c; log in permuter/func_800745EC/).

- id: a-shift-written-in-place-is-retails-in-place-sra | keywords: regalloc, in-place, shift, sra, mask, constant, tie, source shape
  **RETAIL'S `sra rd,rd,0x8` IS AN IN-PLACE C UPDATE (`x = x >> 8`), NOT A SEPARATE RESULT REGISTER, AND WRITING IT THAT WAY FLIPS THE WHOLE PROLOGUE'S REGISTER CONTEST - a 99.00% park went to a match on two `>>=` statements after six declaration/order permutations and two permuter runs measured nothing** (round 437, battling/main/misc9 func_80082488, 99.00 -> 99.92%, XenoBattlingNoGp = gcc-2.7.2 -G0; residual afterwards is reloc spelling only, `check_bin.sh battling` OK). The body takes `x = pPos->vx; z = pPos->vz;`, masks both to cell corners (`mx = x & 0xFFFFFF00`) and shifts both to cell indices. With named results (`col = x >> 8; row = z >> 8;`) retail's prologue was 6 instructions off: mask `$a3` for `$a0`, x `$v0` for `$t2`, z `$a0` for `$t1`, mx/mz mirrored, because each shift's output was a fresh pseudo. Reusing the variable (`z = z >> 8;` for row, then `x = x >> 8;` for col, with the index written `(z << 7) + x`) gives retail's in-place `sra t2,t2,0x8` / `sra t1,t1,0x8` and the mask falls into `$a0` (the register the dying source released) with no other change; the first of the two was found by a 3-minute permuter run (base 255 best 100), the second by applying the same shape to the other shift. So when retail's diff shows an operation DESTROYING its source register, look for the C form that updates in place before running the allocator diagnostics: the -dl/-dg quotient argument this park had accumulated was measuring the fresh pseudos, not the original's shape. Measured dead on the same body: a `VECTOR` local for the 12-byte dot-product view at the dead fourth corner (frame 0x70 -> 0x80) and dropping the three unused `SVECTOR` locals (frame -> 0x58), so those pads are load-bearing.

- id: an-unbounded-array-extern-keeps-a-register-busy | keywords: regalloc, tie, array, extern, scalar, value, temp, delay-slot, phase, lw
  **AN UNBOUNDED ARRAY EXTERN, WHICH HOLDS THE GLOBAL'S ADDRESS IN ONE REGISTER FOR THE WHOLE BODY, ALSO DECIDES WHICH REGISTER THE STORED VALUE TAKES - AND THAT IS WHERE RETAIL'S UNFILLED LOAD-DELAY `nop` COMES FROM** (round 440, field/main/misc8 func_80081C54, 69.69 stale -> 92.75% on the re-measure, 768 bytes / 192 insns against retail's 776 / 194, Default preset). Retail's 17 singleton stores are `lui v1,%hi(idx) / lw v1,%lo(idx)(v1) / nop / sll / addu / lw v1,<field>(p) / sll / lui at / addu at / sw`: the index and the VALUE share `$v1`, so the index load's delay slot cannot be filled (the value load would clobber the address) and stays a `nop`. With the unit's `extern volatile s32 g_FieldSpriteUpdateIndex[]` the body instead holds `&g_FieldSpriteUpdateIndex` in `$a0` for the whole function, loads with `lw v1,0(a0)` (one instruction, retail pays two), and because `$a0` is busy the value takes `$v1` - retail's phase, so every `nop` and every store position matches, but the body ends up 2 instructions SHORT (the two delay slots retail fills from elsewhere and the 8 extra `lui`s it pays). Declaring a scalar alias `extern volatile s32 g_FieldSpriteUpdateCursor __asm__("g_FieldSpriteUpdateIndex")` restores retail's absolute `lui`+`lw` pair per store and the body comes out at retail's EXACT 194 insns / 776 bytes - and scores 71.21 (74.34 with a permuter `i = value` hint) because with the index in `$v1` and `$a0` free the value loads hoist into the delay slot (`lw a0,12(a2)` where retail has `nop`) and every store lands one instruction early. So: when a retail group is `index and value in the same register with a nop between`, both halves of the tie are decided by whether the global's ADDRESS is live - the array extern (register-busy, phase right, one instruction per load short) and the scalar (retail's instruction stream, phase wrong) are two different bodies, not two spellings, and a TU that needs both declaration forms for other functions cannot reach 100. `k-unbounded-array-extern-global` is the same effect read as an address-shape difference.
- id: a-retail-j-after-an-arm-truncation-is-a-ternary-not-a-store-back | keywords: regalloc, a0, ternary, narrow, trunc, select, store-back, caller-saved
  **WHEN RETAIL SHOWS `beq` / `addiu` / `j` / `andi` IN PLACE ON THE ARGUMENT REGISTER, THE BODY IS A TERNARY PASSED AS THE CALL ARGUMENT AND ITS RESULT REGISTER CANNOT BE MOVED TO `$a0` FROM SOURCE - and the spellings that DO reach `$a0` are one instruction short, because gcc then sinks the single narrow conversion past the branch and the whole thing becomes a select** (round 439, menu/main/misc6 func_801D2D38, re-parked 99.74% at retail's exact 392 bytes / 98 insns, XenoMenu260). Retail: `lbu a0,780(at)` (a `u_char` gear id out of a table), `beq a0,s2` / `addiu a0,a0,11` in the delay slot / `j join` / `andi a0,a0,0xff` in the delay slot / else arm `li a0,0xff` / `join: jal`. Every 98-insn spelling (ternary as the argument, arm casts, `& 0xFF`, inverted `!=` form, both arms cast, `gearId` declared in the inner block) is instruction-identical with the value in `$v0` and `andi a0,v0,0xff` at the join; every spelling that assigns the narrow value back to the local (`u_char gearId = cond ? ... : ...;` or if/else with casts per arm, 97.76 / 388 / 97 insns) gives retail's `lbu a0` + in-place `addiu`/`andi` but replaces the `j` with `bne` + one shared `andi` after the `li`, i.e. jump-optimisation merged the two conversions into a select. So the a0 chain and the `j`-with-arm-truncation are mutually exclusive; the reachable part of such a park is the shape (98 insns) and the register is closed - four permuter runs (1650, 991, 1217, 1360 iterations) all base==best, and the two load-bearing neighbours of this body are `characterIndex * 2` with a `u_char` counter (which yields `sll a1,s0,1` + `andi a1,a1,0xFE`) rather than `j * 2` (93.87 / 404), and `6 + (j << 1)` for the second one (94.28 / 400).

- id: a-two-unit-mirror-with-a-25-to-35-percent-quotient-gap-is-closed | keywords: regalloc, callee-saved, mirror, quotient, refs, live, dg, dl, park
  **BEFORE SPENDING A LANE ON A MIRRORED CALLEE-SAVED PAIR IN A FUNCTION THAT IS ALREADY INSTRUCTION-IDENTICAL, COMPUTE THE TWO QUOTIENTS FROM ONE `-da`/`-dg` COMPILE: a 25-35% gap is not the 4%-margin class `a-a-near-miss-allocno-quotient` closes, it is a park** (round 442, world_map/main/misc9, two parks re-measured and quantified: func_800863E0 93.98% at retail's exact 392 bytes and func_80087C6C 97.30% at retail's exact 756 bytes, both XenoWorldMap = gcc-2.7.2 -O2 -G8, both with the whole instruction multiset identical and the only delta one mirrored `$s2`/`$s3`). In func_800863E0 the processed order is `133 138 79 75 130 72 76 73 77 74` = `floor_log2(refs)*refs/live*4`, with 79 = pVertex "16 times across 34 insns" (1.88) ahead of 75 = x "14 times across 28" (1.50), so pVertex takes `$s2` and x is pushed to `$s3`; closing it needs x at 16 refs (a floor_log2 step, i.e. two instructions retail does not have) or 8 fewer live insns. In func_80087C6C the order is `150 79 142 78 73 108 139 147 80 75 74` with 73 -> `$s0` (34/114 = 5.96), 139 = the `g_WorldMapWaypointTileX` symbol_ref -> `$s1` (5/13 = 3.08), 147 = `g_WorldMapWaypointTileZ` -> `$s2` (5/20 = 2.00) and 75 = the `0x1F800000` scratchpad pointer -> `$s3` (17/182 = 1.49); retail has the scratchpad in `$s2`, so the pointer would need live <= 136 insns although its def is in the entry block in BOTH bodies (retail's `lui $s2,0x1f80` sits in the same slot), which no spelling reaches. Two things make this cheap rather than a session: `-dg` prints the process order and the dispositions as `<pseudo> in <hard reg>`, `-dl` prints `<refs> times across <live> insns` per pseudo, and `m.i.cse2` names the pseudos by their RTL (`(set (reg:SI 139) (symbol_ref:SI ("g_WorldMapWaypointTileZ")))`), so the identity of each contested allocno is one grep. Presets screened on both bodies: func_800863E0 gcc-2.6.0 93.78 / cdk 81.80, func_80087C6C 2.6.0 94.81 / -O3 97.30.
- id: a-qiparam-size-one-pins-the-saved-pair | keywords: regalloc, swapped, callee-saved, parameter, qi, size, dg, dl
  **A SWAPPED CALLEE-SAVED PAIR WHOSE LOWER-PRIORITY SIDE IS A `u_char` PARAMETER CANNOT BE FLIPPED: the parameter pseudo is QI, so `allocno_size` is 1 and it carries a 4x handicap in `floor_log2(refs)*refs*10000/(live*size)` that the shifted/temp value on the other side can never make up - read `-dl`'s "N bytes" per pseudo and park in one compile** (round 443, menu/main/misc3 func_801C8D78, re-measured 90.05 -> 97.10% at retail's EXACT 368 bytes / 92 insns, XenoMenu260 = gcc-2.6.0, whole body instruction-identical with retail holding the parameter in `$s2` and `a0 * 0x10` in `$s1` where the build mirrors them). `-da`/`-dl` on the body: `8 regs to allocate: 75 141 87 73 74 71 115 92`, dispositions `73 in 16` (count, QI), `71 in 17` (parameter, "1 bytes"), `115 in 18` (`a0 * 0x10`, SI) give 4655 / 1818 / 394, so retail's arrangement needs 394 to clear 1818 - 4.6x - and every term is pinned by retail's own instruction stream (the parameter stays QI, which is exactly why retail re-does its `andi 0xff` at four separate sites; `a0 * 0x10` spans the `strcpy` loop, live 19). So when a diff shows an input parameter swapped against a computed temporary, dump `-dl` FIRST: a "1 bytes" line on the parameter side ends the lane. On this body, re-ordering declarations and both operand orders of the compares are byte-identical at 97.10.
- id: a-sched2-hoisted-global-address-has-no-source-order | keywords: regalloc, live-length, sched2, hoist, preheader, address, swapped, unreachable
  **WHEN THE LIVE-LENGTH TERM IS THE ONLY THING BETWEEN YOU AND A SWAPPED PAIR, CHECK WHERE THE COMPILER ACTUALLY PUTS THE INIT: a dependency-free `lui/addiu` of a global is hoisted by sched2 to the HEAD of its block, so the source position of the initialising statement does not change the pseudo's live range by one instruction and the `live_length <= N` window is not walkable** (round 443, menu/main/misc3 func_801CBA4C, re-measured 96.29% at retail's exact 836 bytes / 209 insns, XenoMenu260). Retail forms the text walker's `lui/addiu g_GameState` in the FIFTH slot of the loop preheader and this build in the FIRST, so the walker's live range is 36 where retail's own position gives 32, and 32 is inside the `<= 34` window that would flip the pair (see a-a-near-miss-allocno-quotient for that function's 2393-vs-2291 numbers). Moving `pText = (u8*)g_GameState.strings;` from after the three header stores to before them measures the same 96.29 with a byte-identical emission - the preheader order is a CONSEQUENCE of the allocation (sched2's register-pressure model), not a lever on it. So a park note that reads "the address formation happened one statement earlier here" is describing the symptom of the mirror, not a second residual; do not spend a lane re-ordering the preheader.
- id: a-block-split-flips-a-caller-saved-pair | keywords: regalloc, caller-saved, swap, block, do-while, barrier, local-alloc, scheduler
  **A MIRRORED PAIR OF CALLER-SAVED TEMPS (`$v0`/`$v1`) IN A STRAIGHT-LINE BLOCK IS DECIDED BY WHICH BLOCK THE TWO PSEUDOS ARE ALLOCATED IN, NOT ONLY BY THE REFS QUOTIENT: an empty `do { } while (0);` between the two definitions makes local-alloc allocate the first one alone, so it takes `$v0` and the second falls to `$v1` exactly as retail has it - the test costs 15 s and proves in one diff whether the mirror is a wall or a block artefact** (round 452, field/main/misc5 func_800A6E70, still parked 99.56% at retail's exact 500 bytes / 125 insns). Retail holds `j*16`, truncated in place by the `andi`, in `$v0` and that mask + 0x10 in `$v1`; the body compiles the mirror, 18 differing lines, and the seven `-dl`/`-dg` readings and permuter runs in the park note all failed to move it. A `do { } while (0);` between `nU = nX & 0x3F;` and `nU1 = nU + 0x10;` flips it: 98.40%, same 500 bytes / 125 insns, TWO differing lines - the pair is retail's and the cost is the second line, `move a2,s2` (the GetTPage division's dividend copy, which sched2 hoists to the block head right after the SetPolyGT4 delay slot) landing after the `addiu` because sched2 can then only hoist it to the new block. Barrier placements measured on the same body: before the `andi` keeps the pair mirrored and moves the copy below the `andi` (97.96%), a second barrier at the loop-body head changes nothing, and the copy is attached to the LAST barrier - so recovering the copy needs the barrier at the head while the flip needs it after the `andi`, i.e. the two residuals are provably exclusive and no legitimate statement puts a block boundary between the two definitions. Read this as a cheap diagnostic (does the pair flip? then the residual is a block structure question, and any earlier "unreachable quotient wall" verdict in a park note is wrong) and remember the barrier itself is not shippable source, so it can only be used to learn, never to match.


- id: a-global-store-before-the-two-locals-pins-the-missing-li-and-the-s0-s1-roles | keywords: regalloc, callee-saved, swapped, constant, store, order, prologue, hoist
  **WHEN TWO `= 1` LOCALS AND ONE `g = 1` STORE SIT AT THE TOP OF A FUNCTION, THE ORDER OF THOSE THREE STATEMENTS DECIDES BOTH WHICH LOCAL TAKES `$s0`/`$s1` AND WHETHER THE STORE PAYS ITS OWN `li` - and it also decides whether the switch operand's global load gets hoisted above the frame setup** (round 451, menu/main/misc1 func_801C531C, XenoMenu260 = gcc-2.6.0, 161 insns / 644 bytes, matched instruction for instruction with the jtbl operand as the only difference, `check_bin.sh menu` OK). Retail is `ori s1,zero,1` at entry, `lui v1,%hi(g_Menu)` / `lw v1` / `li v0,1` / `sw ra` / `sb v0,g` / `lbu v1,0x336(v1)`, with `ori s0,zero,1` in the `beqz` delay slot; s1 is the returned flag and s0 the per-arm result. Measured on the same body, three orders only: (a) `result = 1; shouldContinue = 1; g = 1;` -> 96.66% / 640 bytes, one instruction short, because the store reused the register already holding 1 (`sb s1,0(at)`), the `g_Menu` load was hoisted ABOVE `addiu sp,sp,-0x20`, and the two locals took the mirrored registers; (b) `g = 1; result = 1; shouldContinue = 1;` -> retail's prologue and the fresh `li v0,1` restored, but the locals still mirrored (`$s0`=returned flag); (c) `g = 1; shouldContinue = 1; result = 1;` -> exact, 644 bytes. So put the global store FIRST and initialise the locals in an order such that the first one gets the HIGHER callee-saved register, and read a store that reuses a saved 1 (`sb s1,<global>`) where retail has `li v0,1` / `sb v0` as this ordering tell rather than as an allocator tie - one reordering, no permuter run needed.

- id: a-retail-recomputes-a-shift-the-sum-also-needs | keywords: regalloc, sll, cse, recompute, duplicate, shift, subu, allocator
  **"RETAIL HAS TWO SHIFT INSTRUCTIONS YOUR BODY DOES NOT" IS A cc1 CSE GAP WHEN THE RECOMPUTED VALUES ARE THE SECOND OPERANDS OF THE SUBTRACTIONS: the `subu` result takes that operand's register, which kills the cse entry the sum needs, and no source spelling reaches it** (round 453, `field/main/misc5` `FieldInitializeParticlePrimitive`, re-measured and re-parked at 96.58% / 512 bytes / 128 insns against retail's 520 / 130). The body writes four values - `x1s-x0s`, `y1s-y0s`, `x0s+x1s`, `y0s+y1s`, `xs = x << 4` - to eight `SVECTOR` halves, i.e. four distinct shifts, and every compile gives four `sll`. Retail has six: `sll a3,a3,4` (x1s) and `sll a1,a1,4` (y1s) are shared with the sums, but in `sll a0,a1,4 / subu a0,a3,a0` the subu's destination IS the second operand's register, so x0s dies there and the sum re-forms it as `sll a1,a1,4`; same for y0s. Ten spellings probed with `cc1_probe.sh` on this unit's own cc1 (all four int locals; `u_short` locals; the four x stores then the four y stores; the shifted extremes in their own locals; a `SVECTOR*` vertex pointer local; a `ParticleShape*` local with inline field reads; no locals with the table read inline at all 16 sites; `x * 16 + y * 16` for the sums; `(x << 4) - -(y << 4)` for the sums): four `sll` every time, except the pointer-local form (the shift lands AFTER the subu) and the inline-read forms (16 `lhu` - the stores invalidate the load entries). -O3 also four, -O1 73.08, cdk 71.36, 2.6.0 identical to Default. Second tell that retail had one more live pseudo at the loads: its four `lhu` start at `a1` with `a0` left free, every spelling here starts at `a0`. Read this with the companion fact of c-a-minus-chain-reassociates-the-subtract (one copy plus one recompute per axis is a cse difference to park) - do not spend a lane probing spellings here, park on the sll count.

## delay slot

symptoms: nop vs instruction in a delay or load slot
try: delay-slot nop jal slot
- id: c-a-hoisted-compare-local-fills-the-branch-slot | keywords: delay-slot, slt, compare, local, hoist, bne, beq, exact-size
  **A `slt` THAT RETAIL PUTS IN A `bne` DELAY SLOT COMES FROM A COMPARISON NAMED IN A LOCAL ABOVE THE `if`, NOT FROM THE `if` CONDITION ITSELF**: `less = x < pSlot->x;` on the statement before `if (x != pSlot->x || z != pSlot->z)`, with `less` used as the arm's test, is what makes reorg fill the `bne` slot and lands retail's exact size; writing `if (x < pSlot->x)` inside the arm instead leaves nops in both delay slots and one extra `slt` later (round 439, battle/main/misc30 func_800B905C, XenoBattleTailCdk0: 96.61% / 512 bytes -> 97.48% / retail's exact 508). Reading the field twice to force retail's two x pseudos (`less = t.xWhole < slot->x; x = t.xWhole;`) costs 8 bytes and 3 points (94.28 / 516). Note retail's second copy of the same `slt` in the FOLLOWING branch's slot is not a source duplicate: the slot-z load clobbers `$v0`, so that recompute is forced once the slt result lives in `$v0` - if yours lands in `$a2` the slot stays a nop and the size is still right.
- id: c-a-store-in-a-jal-delay-slot-proves-source-order | keywords: delay-slot, jal, store, statement-order, reorg, store-before-call
  **AN INSTRUCTION IN A `jal` DELAY SLOT EXECUTES BEFORE THE CALLEE, so a store that sits there in retail proves the source wrote that store BEFORE the call - read the slot before you trust your own statement order, because gcc's reorg does not move a store forward past a call** (round 432, field/main/misc2 func_80076AC0, 73.94 -> 73.82% but 1728 -> 1764 bytes: 9 of the park's 12 missing instructions).
