Retail is `move a0,s0 / lui v0 / lw v0 / ori a1,0x20 / addu v0,s2,v0 / jal SpriteReallocFramesData / sw s0,4(v0)` - the address chain is formed before the call because the STORE is before the call, i.e. `pActorData->pSpriteData = pSpriteData; SpriteReallocFramesData(pSpriteData, 0x20);`. Written call-then-store (the natural reading of the listing) the store address cannot be computed before the call at all - the base is a volatile `lw` - so the whole 5-instruction `sll/addu/sll/subu/sll` index chain is re-formed after the call. In the same body, the sibling path that does NOT end in a call (`j .L80076DC4 / sw s0,4(v1)`) keeps the store AFTER its SpriteAllocFromAnimFile, so the two paths really do differ in order and one statement order cannot serve both.
- id: c-goto-past-a-post-loop-label | keywords: delay-slot, reorg, duplicate, goto, label, block-order, j, short
  **A BODY ONE INSTRUCTION SHORT BECAUSE RETAIL HAS A `j`-OVER-A-BLOCK WHOSE DELAY SLOT DUPLICATES THE NEXT BLOCK'S FIRST INSTRUCTION WANTS AN EXPLICIT `goto` TO A LABEL PLACED AFTER THE LOOP, NOT A BETTER SPELLING OF THE ARM** (round 423, world_map/main/misc10 func_80089748, 97.08 -> 99.89% at retail's exact 332 insns / 1328 bytes, XenoWorldMapDiv). Retail's tail is `[scan exit][sb flags ^= 0x80][store: sll/or/sw]`, i.e. the clear's block sits between the scan exit and the shared store, so the scan exit needs `j store` and reorg fills that slot with a COPY of the store's `sll`. Writing the arm as `else { pSprite->flags ^= 0x80; goto store; }` puts the sb inline in the arm (in the goto's delay slot) and is exactly 4 bytes / 1 insn short. The fix is a second label: `else { goto clearFlags; }` with `clearFlags: pSprite->flags ^= 0x80;` placed AFTER the scan loop and immediately before `store:`, plus an explicit `goto store;` after the scan's if-block so the scan-exit stops falling into the clear (without that goto the body compiles to the right size but CLEARS THE FLAGS ON THE SCAN-EXHAUSTED PATH - wrong code that scores 97.55%). Two companion findings from the same function: `low = p.field; high = low >> 16; low = (s16)low;` (ONE variable for the packed pair) is what puts retail's `lw s5` + `sra s8,s5,16` shape together and fixed the leftover `$s5`/`$s6` mirror; and the residual 0.11% left afterwards is only the scratchpad address form (`%lo(D_1F8000xx)` vs a folded +0xF0 immediate over `s3 = 0x1F800000`), which links identically - do not "fix" it with an extern, misc14 measured that a named D_1F8000xx makes cc1 form a fresh lui+addiu.
- id: c-a-bare-j-tail-call-thunk | keywords: delay-slot, tail, sibcall, j, jal, thunk, frame, noreturn
  **A RETAIL 8-BYTE `j Callee` + `nop` IS A SIBLING CALL, AND NO COMPILER IN tools/ EMITS ONE ON MIPS - so a C wrapper around a variadic callee can never be matched here** (round 406, slus_006.64/system/archive_b func_800379C8, parked 0%, 8 bytes retail). Retail is opcode-2 `j FontPrintf` with a0 untouched: a variadic passthrough that forwards the caller's arguments, which is exactly why the original could not have been an ordinary `f(char* p) { FontPrintf(p); }`. Probed with cc1_probe.sh under the unit's own flags: the call gives `subu sp / sw ra / jal / lw ra / addu sp / j ra` (32 bytes), unchanged by `-foptimize-sibling-calls` and unchanged when the callee is declared `__attribute__((noreturn))` (2.7.2 accepts the attribute and still keeps the frame). The honest variadic signature is worse (48 bytes: the a1-a3 save area). gcc 2.6.0 and 2.7.2-cdk are 0% too, 2.8.1 also frames + jals, and mips.md gets sibcall patterns only in 3.x. So a lone `j`-first glabel is a hand-written thunk: report SIBLING_CALL and do not spend runs on it. (splat prints such a word byte-swapped - 0x0800DC03 shows as `03DC0008` - so verify against disc/ before doubting the disassembly.)
- id: c-a-loop-label-lands-after | keywords: delay-slot, nop, label, mflo, maspsx, branch, displacement, bnez
  **A BACK EDGE WHOSE DISPLACEMENT IS ONE INSTRUCTION SHORT IS maspsx'S HAZARD-NOP PLACEMENT, NOT A SOURCE SHAPE: `_handle_mflo_mfhi` emits the follower and its nop as one unit and only then replays the loop label, so the label lands AFTER the nop where retail's assembler put it BEFORE** (round 404, slus_006.64/system/archive_b func_80037878, parked 99.94% at retail's exact 316 bytes / 79 insns: the whole stream is identical and only `bnez v0,+0x74` vs `+0x78` differs, 2 differing lines). cc1's line order is `mult / mflo / addu / $L8: / mult`; in maspsx's next_next-is-mult branch (tools/maspsx/maspsx/__init__.py) the generic `elif not inst.startswith("#")` path appends the follower, then `res.extend([expand_move(inst), "nop"])` puts the nop after it, and the label line is replayed last - so the three reachable label positions are after the nop (99.94), at the addu (label line moved before the follower, 4 bytes the other way) and before the nops (the next-is-mult branch, which emits TWO nops, +8 bytes). maspsx does use label-then-nop in its OTHER handler (`_handle_nop_before_next_instruction`), so this is one convention apart and only a maspsx edit closes it. Preset screened the same round: -O3 99.94, -G0 99.94, cdk 95.59, 2.6.0 93.54. Diagnose by comparing the branch TARGET against the loop-head nop before re-shaping the loop: several loop spellings (for(;;)+break, for(j=i;...), an empty do/while at the head) all measure the same number with this same single difference.
- id: c-a-call-argument-constant-beats | keywords: delay-slot, li, constant, reorg, jal, priority, scheduling
  **A CALL-ARGUMENT CONSTANT OUTRANKS A STORE-ONLY CONSTANT FOR A BRANCH DELAY SLOT, and no source order reaches it** (round 391, slus_006.64/system/archive func_8002B5D0, stuck at 97.47% / 736 bytes). Retail's `beq` after `CdPosToInt` carries `li $v0,1` (the value stored immediately after the target) where our compile carries `li $a1,0x200` (the following `jal`'s size argument). reorg fills the slot eagerly from the target block when that block is the branch's only successor, taking its FIRST scheduled insn - and sched2's rank_for_schedule compares INSN_PRIORITY first, so the constant that feeds the `jal` (on the block's critical path) is scheduled ahead of the constant that only feeds a `sh`. Statement order, a volatile state store (96.67), a live-in u16 id local (97.47) and a pointer local for the second call argument (97.47) all left the slot unchanged. Symptom to look for: your delay slot holds the arg constant of the FIRST call in the target arm while retail's holds a constant used later in that arm.
  Round 403 re-measured it on func_8002B2F0 (the instruction-identical sibling): still 97.47 / 736 bytes with the exact instruction multiset. New data points on the same body: the two-statement queue index (`entryIndex = g_ArchiveStreamQueueIndex; g_ArchiveStreamQueueIndex = entryIndex + 1;`) scores 97.93 but is 732 bytes / 183 insns - local_alloc loads straight into the callee-saved register and the `move` disappears, so it can never link; a `pCurrent = NULL` initialiser breaks the duplicated tail's cross-jump (92.50 / 740 / 185). permute.sh cannot run the body at all: its parser reports 'conversion to non-scalar type requested' on the write-pointer read line for all three spellings tried (inline `s8* volatile*` cast, `s8* volatile*` local, `volatile u32*` local), so this park is hand-only.
  Round 431 on the same body: the DOUBLE NEGATION on the destination sum (`entryIndex * CD_SECTOR_SIZE - (-(mem_addr)*(s8* volatile*)&writePointer)`, the c-double-negation mechanism) keeps the score at 97.47 / 736 but lands the `sll` in `$a0` as retail does and removes the maspsx load-delay `nop`, so the residual becomes a PURE REORDER - identical multiset, identical registers, and the only moved positions are this section's `li` pair and the `sll`'s slot. That is the closest shape measured on this pair and is what both parks now carry. Also re-measured on it: both id reads plain CSE to one `lhu` (96.40 / 732 / 183, so the volatile cast is load-bearing); permuting the four found-block statements in source (id read hoisted into a `u16` local, state store after it) compiles BYTE-IDENTICALLY, which is the proof that these two rows are scheduler priorities and not statement order; gcc-2.7.2-cdk 95.67.
- id: c-twin-source-shape-beats-a-park | keywords: delay-slot, twin, park, load-slot, addiu, pointer, increment
  **A PARKED BODY THAT DIFFERS FROM RETAIL ONLY IN "PLACEMENT" IS A DIFFERENT SOURCE SHAPE, AND A MATCHING TWIN IN THE SAME UNIT HANDS YOU THE SHAPE - port it verbatim before re-deriving it** (round 417, menu/main/misc7_r2 func_801D6338, 309 insns under XenoMenu260: the park stood at 95.53% at retail's exact 1236 bytes with two delay-slot residuals, and a wholesale port of the matching twin func_801D680C's body reached 100.00 / 1236 bytes on the FIRST diff run - the two residuals disappeared without being separately diagnosed, so this lever is the method, not a bisected attribution). The three shape differences the park had, all of them exactly the kind that produces a slot difference: (1) the digit scan was `for (i = 0; i < count; ) { u8 digit = digits[digitOffset + i++]; if (digit != NONE) ... }` where the twin has `for (i = 0; i < count; i++) { if (digits[digitOffset + i] != NONE) ... }` and uses the same subexpression as the call's index - the post-increment in the index makes gcc put a `nop` in the `lw $a2,%lo(g_Menu)` load-delay slot where retail fills it with `addu v0,digitOffset,i`; (2) the prim-address argument was an integer sum with the base added FIRST (`count * (2*sizeof(POLY_FT4)) + 0x1630 + (u32)base`), which emits `addiu t0,t0,0x1630` on the base and a plain `addu a2,a2,t1`, where the twin's pointer form `(POLY_FT4*)((u8*)base + (count * (2*sizeof(POLY_FT4)) + 0x1630))` keeps 0x1630 in the scaled-count chain and puts the base add in the `jal` delay slot - retail's shape, with the base/count temps also swapping registers (t1/t0) as a consequence; (3) the x cursor was `x - characterId * 24` with `x += MENU_DIGIT_SPACING` at the loop end, where the twin keeps `x + i * MENU_DIGIT_SPACING - characterId * 24` and gcc strength-reduces it into the back-edge `addiu s1,s1,8`. So when a park's note reads "same instruction multiset, only the placement differs", run twin_for.py and diff the twin's body against the park before trying spellings by hand.
- id: c-there-source-ordering-lever | keywords: delay-slot, nop, li, delay, goto, jr, reorg
  **There is no source ordering lever for delay-slot fill** (round 311, ten micro probes): reorg always steals the instruction immediately preceding `jr` in RTL, so a ternary, a comma expression, a goto, an if/else and a scoped block all measure identical. What moves is the RTL position of a `li v0,K` return against trailing constant-zero stores, and with TWO constant-zero stores before `return 1` the `li` is still the one stolen. If a park's only residual is which instruction fills a slot, park it with that number and say so instead of spending the batch.
- id: c-load-load-sub-against | keywords: delay-slot, nop, delay
  **`load/load/sub` against retail's `load/nop/sub` is a DECLARATION-ORDER question, not a scheduling one.** round 370, battling/misc5 func_8007B270 (85.17 -> 100.00): dbr_schedule fills a load-delay slot from a LATER independent insn, so the order in which the channel locals are assigned decides which load gets stolen - the natural ascending `r = p0[0] - 0x80; g = p0[1] - 0x80; b = p0[2] - 0x80;` produced retail's shape where reordering the STORES never moved the tie.
- id: c-original-keeps-li-a0 | keywords: delay-slot, nop, li, delay, jal, move, reorg
  The original keeps `li a0,N` in a `jal`'s delay slot while yours hoists it: gcc has no scheduler here, so the `li` is stolen into the slot by reorg only when it is the last insn before the call and no register conflict blocks it. Move other argument setup earlier, or make the constant argument the last one evaluated.
- id: c-a-call-argument-constant-folds-early-when-the-compare-load-sinks | keywords: delay-slot, li, constant, jal, nop, load, compare
  **A CALL WHOSE RESULT IS COMPARED AGAINST A LONE NARROW LOAD FIRES ITS ARGUMENT CONSTANT ~9 INSTRUCTIONS EARLY AND NO SPELLING MOVES IT, so retail's `jal; ori a0,N` slot is unreachable on that shape while the identical shape one function away matches** (round 443, field/main/misc11 func_80095CC4, parked 96.19% / 172 bytes / 43 insns against retail's 168 / 42 - the whole body, the registers and the branch layout are exact, the only extra insn is the `nop` that takes the slot the argument constant should have filled). `FieldScriptVMGetArgument(2)`'s `li $4,2` comes out right after the previous call in cc1's OWN output (`build/src/<unit>.c.s`, so this is not the assembler), before the array-index sll chain, and reorg then fills the call's slot with a nop. Measured, all 172 bytes: plain `arg = F(2); if (arg == p->field)` 96.19; an `int wm = p->field;` local hoisted above the call compiles BYTE-IDENTICALLY (copy-propagated) so the load's statement order is not the lever, 96.19; negated test with the arms swapped byte-identical, 96.19; operands swapped (`p->field == arg`) puts the load BEFORE the call and drops to 94.76; gcc-2.7.2-cdk `-O2 -G8` 96.19. The MATCHED neighbour FieldScriptCheckActorWalkmeshMaterial has retail's exact `jal; ori a0,2` slot, and the one difference is that its compared value is an `lbu` reached through the triangle table (so it is emitted before the call) where this one is a lone `lh 0x10(s0)`. Park this class rather than permuting below 99%.
- id: c-only-difference-hoisted-li | keywords: delay-slot, nop, delay, jal, li
  Only difference is a hoisted `li a0,N` where the original keeps it in the jal delay slot: put the argument in an `int` local before the call, or pass a variable instead of a literal.
- id: c-stolen-delay-slot-retail | keywords: delay-slot, nop, addiu, andi, delay, reorg, sll
  A stolen delay slot (retail has a `nop` where yours has an `andi`/`sll`/`addiu`): the cheapest lever found is the function's RETURN TYPE - `void` lets gcc 2.7.2/2.6.0 reorg steal the instruction into the slot, a non-void type (`int`, `char`, even unused) keeps retail's `nop` (rounds 64-65 closed three functions this way, one of them 99.36% -> 100.00% with no other change). Try that before any statement reordering, and say in a comment why the unused return type is there.
- id: c-make-gcc-put-return | keywords: delay-slot, nop, jr, addu, delay, move
  To make gcc put a return-value copy in the jr $31 delay slot (retail's jr $31 / addu $2,$5,$0 instead of move $2,$5 hoisted to the top), put "$2" in the asm block's clobber list; without it the copy floats above the block (+4 bytes, 98.x%). maspsx also REJECTS hex immediates inside an inline-asm body (invalid literal for int() with base 10: '0x6') - write them decimal, as the matched ScaleMatrix/SetMulMatrix blocks do.
- id: c-two-stores-same-value | keywords: delay-slot, nop, delay, lw, sw
  Two stores of the same value class: SOURCE ORDER decides which one gets the load-delay slot. Retail `lw v0,112(s1); sw v1,4(s2)` (constant store in the delay slot) needs the non-constant store written FIRST (round 47, world_map/misc8 func_80082F64: 98.71 -> 100%).
- id: c-wrong-parameter-type-callee | keywords: delay-slot, nop, addiu, andi, delay, jal, reorg, s32
  A WRONG PARAMETER TYPE ON THE CALLEE COSTS A DELAY SLOT: declaring a callee's first parameter s32 instead of u_char removes an andi a0,a0,0xff at every call site, which is what lets reorg put the following addiu in the jal slot (menu/misc12's func_801E8B4C). Before blaming your caller, check the callee's prototype against its own .s.
- id: c-dead-constant-delay-slot | keywords: delay-slot, nop, delay, cse, reorg, missing, arm, eager
  **FALSIFIED IN ROUND 404 - READ THIS BEFORE BELIEVING THAT A CONSTANT IN A DELAY SLOT IS "DEAD".** The 99.87% park of field/dialogue/text_box func_8009C5A8 (round 304, "a dead ori $s0,zero,0x94 that this build declines to place") was a MISSING LIVE ASSIGNMENT, not a reorg fill: the below-actor arm's outer `else { width = 0x48; height = 4; x = 0xA0; }` never set `y`, so a `y = 0x94;` spelled first in that block closes the function at 100.00% (1872 bytes, 468 insns) - retail's ori sits in the bne delay slot because reorg's EAGER fill moves the FIRST instruction of a branch target that the branch is the only reference to. The tell that round 304 missed: the value is READ on the taken path (the else arm falls through to the layout clamps, which consume `y`), so it was never dead; and the sibling arm's else had its own `y = 0x10;` in the same position, which is the shape to compare against. When a one-instruction delay-slot residual is a constant store/li, first check whether every arm of the corresponding if/else sets that variable in your C, and whether the arms are symmetric; only call it dead after proving no path reads it.
- id: c-eager-fill-steals-the-targets-arg0-copy | keywords: delay-slot, nop, missing, move, argument, eager, reorg, if-else
  **WHEN BOTH ARMS OF AN `if`/`else` CALL THE SAME FUNCTION, gcc EXPANDS `arg0 = pSpriteData` BEFORE THE OTHER ARM'S LOAD, SO THE ELSE BLOCK'S RTL STARTS WITH THE COPY AND reorg's EAGER FILL HOISTS IT INTO THE `bnez` AT THE TOP - retail's block starts with the `lw`, which eager fill cannot take, so retail keeps the `nop` and re-forms the copy at the arm; the function then compiles ONE INSTRUCTION SHORT and can never link** (round 443, `slus_006.64/system/temp1` func_80022B2C, parked 96.25% / 376 bytes against retail's 380 / 96 insns, XenoGraphics). Retail's else arm is `lw a1,16(s0)` / `addu a0,s0` / `jal` / `sra a1,a1,4`; every body emits `addu a0,s0` first, reorg moves it to the `bnez` delay slot, and the arm loses the copy. Measured dead for reaching the load-first order: hoisting `speed = pSpriteData->step.y;` as the else body's first statement (`... SpriteScaleValue(pSpriteData, speed >> 4)`) compiles BYTE-IDENTICALLY to the inline form - gcc folds the local back into the argument expansion - and so does declaring the else's load in its own function-scope local. The second residual on the same body is reorg's other direction: a `speed = -step.y * flags / 256; step.y = speed;` whose quotient retail keeps in the mflo register (`sra v1,v1,8`) gets a fresh register here (`sra a1,v1,8`) and reorg then moves the following `sw` into the `bgez` delay slot where retail has a `nop` (a separate `bounce` local instead of reusing `speed` scores 95.99 with the value in `$v0` and gravity in `$a0`, retail: bounce `$v1`, gravity `$v0`). Preset is settled by an earlier 2.6.0/2.7.2/-O1..-O3/-G4..-G8 sweep; park this class with the two mechanisms named rather than re-briefing spellings.
- id: c-a-displaced-pointer-load-pair | keywords: delay-slot, nop, load, hoist, deferred, base, schedule-insns, pair
  **TWO ADJACENT POINTER LOADS IN RETAIL WHERE YOUR BUILD DEFERS ONE OF THEM IS OUTSIDE BOTH SCHEDULING FLAGS, SO PROBE cc1 ONCE (5 s) BEFORE SPENDING DIFF RUNS ON OPERAND ORDERS** (round 408, field/main/misc12 func_80099AC0, parked 97.96% at 1076 bytes against retail's 1080 - one load-delay `nop` short). Retail's case-2 arm is `subu; sll v1,v1,2; lui a0,%hi(Base); lw a0; lui a1,%hi(Cur); lw a1; addu v1,v1,a0; lw s0,0x4C(v1); nop; lhu v0,0x1E(s0); lhu a0,0x1E(a1)`: the base pointer load is deferred BELOW the index's last `sll` and the second pointer load sits adjacent to it, so gas has nothing to pad after `lw s0`. Every body measured loads the base BEFORE that `sll` and the second pointer AFTER `lw s0`, which fills the slot. The probe that closes the question without editing the unit: run the unit's own cc1 on the already-built `build/src/<unit>.c.i` with the unit's `cc_flags` plus `-fno-schedule-insns -fno-schedule-insns2` inside the build container - the non-scheduled stream is byte-for-byte the same order, so it is not sched1 or sched2, and the preset screen agrees (2.6.0 95.27, cdk 96.74 with the same deferred load, -O3 fails to compile). Report it parked with the cc1 probe in the note instead of permuting: three earlier permuter runs found nothing and the target-first operand order only re-ranks objdiff's fuzzy score (97.72) while the byte count stays 4 short.
    - round 429 on the same function, two spellings the 408 note did not cover: (1) target-first written as `(FIELD_ACTORS + FieldScriptVMGetActorIndex(1))->pActorData` with `pTargetActor->solidRange + g_FieldScriptVMCurActor->solidRange` DOES reach retail's `lhu` pair order and `addu a0,v0,a0` (97.72, still 1076 bytes, the pointer loads stay displaced), so target-first is the right operand order and the byte loss is the pointer placement alone; (2) reading the second pointer through a `volatile` cast (`(*(ActorData* volatile*)&g_FieldScriptVMCurActor)->solidRange`) does NOT stop cc1 sinking that load into the `lw s0` load-delay slot, so the placement is not source-order-reachable even with a volatile barrier - do not retry volatile here.
    - round 452 (slus_006.64/psyq/libgpu MoveImage, PSY-Q preset, parked 95.69% / 196 bytes / 49 insns = retail's exactly): the SAME probe answers the opposite way here, so run it before parking a one-slot-moved load. Retail keeps the call's arg0 load (`lw a0,0x18(v1)`) AFTER the last packet store; cc1 hoists it four slots to just below `sw a0,0(a1)`. Compiling the body's own `.i` with the unit cc1 plus `-fno-schedule-insns -fno-schedule-insns2` moves BOTH vtable loads to the end in RTL order (`lw 8(v1)` then `lw 24(v1)`, where retail has 24 then 8) and reorders the prologue saves, so here the hoist IS sched1's and no flag or preset reproduces retail - the two states bracket retail and neither is reachable. Also measured: reversing the source store order (`D_80056984` before `*pPacket`) keeps the same hoist and drops to 87.33, so the store order in the diff is sched-chosen and reordering statements only loses the store block retail already matches.
- id: c-call-arg-setup-swapped | keywords: delay-slot, jal, addiu, move, argument, statement, hoist
  **A TWO-INSTRUCTION ARGUMENT-SETUP SWAP BEFORE A `jal` CLOSES WHEN THE COMPUTED ARGUMENT BECOMES A STATEMENT ABOVE ITS `if`** (round 389, battle/misc7 func_8007C040, 97.53 -> 99.78 at retail's exact 356 bytes). Retail had `beqz s2,L` / `addiu a1,s2,-1` in the slot / `move a0,zero` / `jal` / `andi a1,a1,0xff`; the body `BattleSlotBitMask(buf[GameRandomByteRange(0, count - 1)])` inside `if (count != 0)` gave `move a0,zero` in the branch slot and the addiu after it, because gcc expands call arguments left to right. Moving the bound out - `lastIndex = count - 1;` as a statement BEFORE the `if`, then `GameRandomByteRange(0, lastIndex)` - puts the addiu first and matches. A `s32 last = count - 1;` DECLARATION inside the if (or inside a nested block) does not; the permuter found it in 1274 iterations after two hand spellings measured identical.
- id: c-s32-return-type-otherwise | keywords: delay-slot, nop, s32, addiu, callee-saved, delay, li, reorg
  AN s32 RETURN TYPE ON AN OTHERWISE VOID FUNCTION can be the last instruction (round 305): with void, reorg steals the branch target's addiu into the final beqz slot where retail has a nop; declaring the function s32 (and not using the value) puts the nop back. Also from the same body: a && with the else arm as the fall-through is what lets gcc delay-slot-fill a li and drop a j where the || spelling costs an extra jump; and a type = 0; STATEMENT after a store beats a declaration initialiser, which keeps the variable live across the first call and costs a callee-saved register.
- id: c-select-copy-in-branch | keywords: delay-slot, select, polarity, beqz, reorg, return, permuter
  **RETAIL'S SELECT WITH THE VALUE COPY IN THE BRANCH'S OWN DELAY SLOT NEEDS THE `-1` ARM AS THE FALL-THROUGH, AND WHAT PINS IT IS A DEAD `do { } while` LOOP** (round 392, slus_006.64/psyq/libsn PCcreate and _SN_read, both 100.00 at retail's exact 32 and 24 bytes, after both had been parked at 84-99% for rounds). Retail is `beqz v0,L; addu v0,v1,zero; addiu v0,zero,-1; L: jr ra`: branch on the status the break syscall left in $v0, the value copy speculated into that branch's delay slot, the -1 arm in the fall-through. Two returns alone (`if (status == 0) return value; return -1;`) give the MIRROR image - `bnez v0,L; li v0,-1` in the slot, `move v0,v1` in the fall-through, same six words, 56.67%. What flips it is comparing the status against a LOCAL holding 0 and leaving a dead loop in the -1 arm: `noError = 0; if (status != noError) { return -1; do { } while (noError); } else return value;`. The loop is load-bearing (delete it and the mirror comes back) and a literal `if (status != 0)` does not substitute for the local. permute.sh found it in 21 iterations from the mirror body after ~30 hand shapes (if/else both ways, ternary, goto join, hard-bound registers, 2.6.0/cdk/-O2) had failed; every break-syscall wrapper in that unit is the same six words.
- id: c-two-loads-hoist-the | keywords: delay-slot, li, constant, hoist, load, lbu, block
  **A BLOCK WITH TWO LOADS GETS ITS FIRST CONSTANT ASSIGNMENT HOISTED TO THE BLOCK TOP AND ITS SECOND DROPPED INTO THE FIRST LOAD'S DELAY SLOT, whatever the statement order** (round 393, menu/main/misc4_260 func_801CDC6C, parked 99.17 at retail's exact 1120 bytes / 280 insns). Retail's `if`/`else` prologue is `andi/sll chain, lbu gearId, li count, sll chain, lw hp, li base` - each constant in the delay slot of the load above it and NOTHING at the block top - and six source orders all gave the same 1-at-top/1-after-the-lbu shape: (value,base,count) 99.17, (value,count,base) 99.15, `u8 gearId` declaration first 99.15, and a `do { ... } while (0)` around the arm 98.75 (it also reshuffles s4/s6/s7/s5). A `cc1_probe.sh` replica (a byte load feeding a word load, plus two constant assignments) isolates the trigger: with ONE load in the block both `li`s stay after it in source order, with the second load present the first `li` moves to the block top. So when a park's only residual is which side of a load two `li`s sit on, probe the block with one load removed before spending more orders on it - the placement is reorg's, not the C's. permute.sh 3 minutes: base=90 best=90.
  Round 407 named the pass: it is the PRE-RELOAD scheduler, not reorg. Compiling the same `.i` with the unit's own cc1 and `-fno-schedule-insns2` keeps the first `li` at the block top, `-fno-schedule-insns` moves it to just before the byte load, and with BOTH off the block ends `lw, li base, li count` (retail's stream order) and reorg fills the two load-delay slots. So a park of this shape is a cc1 SCHEDULING artifact that no statement order reaches - it is not worth a second permutation round, and it is not evidence that the preset is wrong (the same function's other residual, a `sw s5/move s5,a3` + `sw s8/move s8,a1` parameter-copy pair that retail emits in NON-parm order, behaves the same way under all four flag sets). Round 447 saw the same class in battle/main/misc15 func_8008BED8 (parked 99.42% / 1156 against retail 1160): retail's `sw s4,88(sp); li s4,1` pair sits right after `li s6,2` only when the `pagePending = 1;` statement is in the init block, and EVERY position after the two `BattleInitSlotGraphics` calls (between them, after both, after the next call) collapses to one body that puts the pair five slots late, after `li a3,0x118` - so when one post-call position has been measured, the others are the same body and statement order is exhausted.
- id: c-an-empty-do-while-does-not-pin-a-li-below-a-load | keywords: delay-slot, li, constant, hoist, load, do-while, barrier
  **THE ROUND-433 EMPTY `do { } while (0)` BARRIER PINS A `li` BELOW A STORE (and orders two memory refs against each other, see `c-a-per-site-do-while-restores-the-load-delay`) BUT NOT A `li` AGAINST A LOAD: a block whose only residual is a constant that sched1 hoists to the block top is byte-identical with the barrier inserted** (round 436, menu/main/misc4_260 func_801CDC6C, re-parked 99.29% at retail's exact 1120 bytes / 280 insns, XenoMenu260). Two spellings measured on that body: the empty `do { } while (0)` between the value load and the base assignment changes nothing, and writing the else arm as its own `gearId` statement, then `glyphCount`, then `value`, then `glyphBase` - retail's exact STREAM order, the order that reproduces retail instruction for instruction once sched1 is off - measures 99.26 with FOUR differing lines, because sched1 hoists the first `li` (count) to the block top and drops the second (base) into the `lbu` delay slot. So `c-two-loads-hoist-the`'s "whatever the statement order" holds even when the order is provably retail's, and `-O1` (the only preset-visible way to turn sched1 off) is 69.74 on the same body: park, do not re-permute.
- id: c-moved-constant-merges-then-sinks | keywords: delay-slot, nop, li, constant, cse, sink, load
  **MOVING A CONSTANT ASSIGNMENT UP OUT OF A LOAD-DELAY SINK MERGES IT WITH THE SAME CONSTANT'S STORE AND COSTS AN INSTRUCTION** (round 396, menu/main/misc9 func_801E05D0, parked 98.88% / 2468 bytes against retail 2472 - 4 bytes short). Retail's inner-switch tail has `lui v0,g_Menu / lw v0,0(v0) / nop / lw v0,0x33C(v0)` plus a separate `li s0,1` for `closeList` ten insns earlier, right after the `MenuCopyEquipmentToPanel` call; the natural body (`shouldRender[0] = 1;` then `closeList = 1;`) keeps BOTH `li 1`s but reorg drops closeList's into that load slot, so nothing is left for the assembler to pad and the function is one insn short. Every order that puts `closeList = 1;` before the store makes cse merge the two QI constants (615 insns / 2464 bytes / 98.39 - the store becomes `sb s0,0x140`), and before the call itself breaks the arm (620 insns / 93.26); this is the `c-two-loads-hoist-the` class seen from the other side, so park it rather than permute. Round 432 re-measured the function (the note's 98.88 / 2468 / 617 is current under gcc-2.6.0) and added one spelling: `closeList = 1;` FIRST, then the cursor-prim call, then the `shouldRender` store - moving the store below the call does stop the merge (both `li 1`s survive) but cc1 then sinks the store below the call (`jal` / `lw g_Menu` / `#nop` / `lw 0x33C` / `li s0` / `sb s0,0x140`), so the arm loses retail's shape and the body is still 617 / 2468 / 98.00. The lead for a next attempt is that the SAME function's BACK arm already reproduces retail exactly - cc1 hoists its `li s0,1` above the cursor-prim argument setup and keeps both `li 1`s - so the hoist is reachable in this unit and only the CONFIRM arm's cse merge stands in the way.

- id: c-call-result-copy-needs | keywords: delay-slot, jal, move, copy, callee-saved, temp, statement
  **A CALL RESULT'S COPY INTO A CALLEE-SAVED REGISTER LANDS IN THE NEXT CALL'S `jal` DELAY SLOT ONLY WHEN THE `+ K` THAT CONSUMES IT IS A SEPARATE ASSIGNMENT THROUGH A SECOND LOCAL** (round 396, field/main/misc func_8008A2E8, 98.33 -> 100.00 at retail's exact 504 bytes / 126 insns). Retail is `jal FieldScriptArgument1 / li a0,5` then `li a0,4 / move a1,zero / jal ArchiveSetIndex / move s0,v0 / addiu s0,s0,2043`: reorg fills the slot from the insn immediately preceding the jump in RTL, which is the result copy only if the copy is the LAST thing before the argument setup. Three probe spellings of `x = f(); g(); ...` separate it: `x += 0x7FB;` after the call puts the copy right after `f` and `move a1,zero` in the slot (98.33, our shape); `x = f() + 0x7FB;` folds to one `addu s0,v0,2043` (4 bytes short); `t = f(); g(); x = t + 0x7FB;` with a SECOND local keeps the separate `move s0,v0` in the slot plus the separate `addiu` and matches. The second local must be its own variable: reusing one that another arm of the same `if` writes widens its live range, moves the copy out of the slot AND flips that other arm's register (97.38 / 508 bytes).

- id: c-displaced-li-inside | keywords: li, constant, hoist, store, group, sched2, priority, preset, sweep
  **A SINGLE `li` AT THE WRONG DISTANCE INSIDE A STRAIGHT-LINE CONSTANT-STORE GROUP IS A SCHEDULING TIE NO SPELLING REACHES - SWEEP THE PRESETS AND PARK** (round 397, field/main/misc9 func_800AC3AC, parked 97.89% at retail's exact 1520 bytes / 380 instructions: every other instruction, the frame and the saved set identical). Retail keeps `ori $v1,zero,0x280` at the head of the 56-store group (60 insns before its first `sh`); the body emits it 18 before, right after the `0x18` def. The other three defs (0xFF, 0x18, 0xC8) land exactly where retail has them in BOTH builds, so the hoisting is register-/priority-driven and only this one lost its slot. Measured dead: every local spelling of the constant (declaration initialiser, statement assignment at the group head, s16/u16 casts, store-to-load-forwarded reuse), `do {} while (0)` around the group (94.64) or around the zero stores (75.68), moving the stores up (93.73 - sched2 does not re-sort stores), -O3, gcc 2.6.0 (97.82), 2.7.2-cdk (70.15), and `try_flags.sh --sweep` (2.7.2 -O2/-O3 x -G4/-G8 x mcpu 3000/r8000), which reads 97.89 in every cell. A `cc1_probe.sh` replica reproduces the body's shape, so retail's def was RTL-positioned at the block head - its source had a statement there that cse folds in this build. Park, do not permute. CONFIRMED on a 39-insn pure-initialiser (round 441, battle/main/misc24 func_800AA898, XenoBattle260Div, parked 74.36% at retail's exact 156 bytes / 39 insns): retail's `addiu v1,zero,-1` is insn 1, ahead of a 0xFFFF/0xFF/0x6B `li`+store triplet, and every body emits it at slot 5 inside the triplet, all 39 instructions otherwise in retail's order - so objdiff's 74.36 is ONE displaced load, not a structural distance. A local cannot hoist it: `s32 negOne = -1;` used by all three -1 stores (cse rematerialises a constant home at its first use) and `s16 n = -1;` both leave it at slot 5, and `try_flags.sh --sweep` over THIS body reads -O1 94.87 / -O2,-O3 74.36 in all 24 cells of 2.6.0 / 2.7.2 / 2.7.2-cdk x -G4/-G8 x 3000/r8000. The identical function in lib_gear (func_801E3534) is parked for the same residual. One clue for a future round: drop the triplet and the same body hoists both homes to slots 0 (-1) and 1 (1), so the retail -1 home was created at the block head by a statement cse folds away.

- id: a-split-the-if-arm-at-the-product | keywords: delay slot, mult, mflo, sll, sra, split, statement, permuter
  **A `mult`/`mflo` PAIR THAT RETAIL FILLS WITH TWO SHIFT INSTRUCTIONS COMES FROM A SPLIT STATEMENT, NOT FROM ONE NESTED EXPRESSION** (round 400, world_map/misc16 func_80099708, 81.95 -> 91.34 and then 94.82 at retail's exact 260 bytes / 65 insns). Retail is `mult v0,t5 / sll v1,v1,0x18 / sra v1,v1,0x15 / mflo s1 / sra v0,s1,0x14 / addu`; written as one expression (`c = (((pTbl[i].sin * iCol) >> 20) + ((s8)v << 3)) << 16;`) cc1 keeps the mult and the shifts adjacent and leaves the latency unfilled, which costs a whole different body. Two statements - `c = (s8)v << 3;` then `c = ((prod >> 20) + c) << 16;` with `prod` its own local - emit the shifts between `mult` and `mflo` and everything else falls into place. The permuter found the split; it is a normal thing a programmer writes, so port it.

- id: c-a-strength-reduced-index-bump | keywords: delay-slot, nop, addiu, loop, biv, conditional, increment, x
  **AN `addiu sN,sN,8` RETAIL KEEPS INSIDE THE LOOP BODY WHILE ITS LOOP-BACK SLOT HOLDS A `nop` MEANS THE ORIGINAL BUMPED THE COORDINATE ITSELF, CONDITIONALLY - the `x + i * 8` call argument that reproduces the sibling loop cannot produce it, because loop.c turns `i * 8` into an unconditional biv increment and reorg then drops it into the back-edge delay slot** (round 410, menu/main/misc7_r2 func_801D680C, park 99.62 -> 100.00 at retail's exact 1256 bytes / 314 insns). The function draws two number rows with the same `for (i = 0; i < count; i++) if (digits[...] != NONE) primCount += func(..., x + i * 8, y, 0x1000);` shape; row 1 matches with that spelling (its bump is in the `bnez` slot, exactly as retail has it) and row 2 does not: retail row 2 is `lbu <count> / addiu s1,s1,8 / addu / sb` inside the `if` with a `nop` in the back-edge slot. Writing row 2 as `func(..., x - characterId * 24, y, 0x1000); x += MENU_DIGIT_SPACING;` with the bump as the LAST statement of the `if` block makes sched2 place the addiu between the count's reload and its add and leaves the back-edge slot empty - the whole 4-line residual vanished in one edit. The two rows really do differ in the source (row 1's bump executes on every iteration, row 2's only on a drawn digit), so do not 'clean up' the asymmetry; it is invisible in behaviour because `x` is dead after the loop. Symptom: a lone `nop`/`addiu` swap in a loop-back slot where the same function has a second loop that matches.

- id: c-a-per-site-do-while-restores-the-load-delay | keywords: delay-slot, nop, load-delay, do-while, store, order, block, barrier
  **A `do { pActor = g_FieldScriptVMCurActor; read; } while (0)` BARRIER BLOCK AROUND EACH CONTINUATION READ - with the store of the previous result left OUTSIDE it - RESTORES RETAIL'S MISSING BASE-LOAD `nop` AND ITS EXACT INSTRUCTION COUNT, BUT THE STORE STILL LANDS BEFORE THE ACTOR LOAD AND ANY STRAIGHT-LINE TAIL AFTER THE BLOCKS SCRAMBLES** (round 411, field/main/misc func_8008EA58, 114 -> 118 insns at retail's exact 118 / 472 bytes, 74.57 -> 65.84 because the tail reorders, so measure both ends). Five script-argument reads: retail emits per site `[lui/lw v1 actor][sh store of the previous result][lhu v0,0xCC(v1)][lui/lw v1 scriptData][nop][addu v0,v0,v1][lbu]`, i.e. the store sits between the actor load and the ip read and the base load's delay slot has nothing left to fill. The natural spelling (`pActor = g_FieldScriptVMCurActor;` then the store, then the next read) makes cc1 load scriptData BEFORE the ip lhu and fill that lw's slot with the `sh`, so every site is one instruction short (114 insns, 74.57%) and can never link. Wrapping each continuation read together with its `pActor` assignment in its own `do {...} while (0)` gives every site the base-load nop and the exact byte count (each row then reads `[sh store][lw actor][lw base][lhu][nop][addu][lbu]`, still one swap from retail's actor-load-first order); the matched twin FieldScriptSetGameState17FC30 gets the same order for free because its store is a struct field and loading `g_pGameState` pins it. Reach for this when a park is N instructions short with exactly one missing load-delay nop per repeated read/store pair.
Round 425 settled the split: wrap ONLY the read, keep `pActor = g_FieldScriptVMCurActor;` and the store of the previous result OUTSIDE the block (`pActor = ...; store; do { value = call(n, SCRIPT_READ_U8(pActor->ip + 0xB)); } while (0);`) - then every site is retail's `[lw actor][sh store][lhu ip][lw base][nop][addu][lbu]` and the function reaches retail's exact 118 insns / 472 bytes, 74.57 -> 83.85. So the block boundary is what pins the order (cc1 keeps it; the base load cannot be hoisted across it), and the head of this class of function is solvable even though the do-while is not shippable C - report it ROUGH. Same function, same round: statement POSITION inside a single straight-line tail block is not a lever at all (a read-modify-write moved to the first, middle and last position of a store group measures byte-identical, as does an explicit early `u16 t = G;` read with a late `G = t & 0xF;`), so when a tail's remaining diff is three instruction positions, the cause is the register one constant got, and the permuter's own score is not objdiff's: two runs improved the permuter objective 2165 -> 1175 -> 985 while objdiff went 83.85 -> 82.92 -> 74.41 on the same 472 bytes.
- id: c-both-sides-of-the-store-get-the-block | keywords: delay-slot, nop, load-delay, do-while, volatile, pointer, chain, block
  **THE `do { } while (0)` BLOCK AROUND A REPEATED READ OF A `T* volatile` GLOBAL HAS TO CONTAIN THE STORE TOO, AND IT IS PER-STATEMENT: four consecutive `pActor->f = g_FieldActors[i].g << 16;` statements are 8 INSTRUCTIONS SHORT (retail emits each as its own serial `lui/lw/nop/addu/lw/nop/sll/sw` chain) until each one gets its own block** (round 428, field/main/misc8 func_80080A74, 77.88 -> 82.88%, 1200 -> 1228 bytes of retail's 1232, 300 -> 307 of 308 insns, Default preset). A wrapper holding only the read side measures nothing; blocks that are too big also lose: one block around the neighbouring curTriNormal triple is +2 insns (1240, 82.80, it re-reads walkmeshId once more) and one block around the flags12C/flags130 mask statements drops to 70.81 because the hoisted mask constants lose their registers. Wrap exactly one read-plus-store statement per block, and expect the same recipe NOT to transfer to the adjacent straight-line code - measure each block boundary.
- id: c-an-empty-do-while-in-a-case | keywords: delay-slot, reorg, duplicate, andi, switch, dispatch, block, do-while, barrier
  **AN EMPTY `do { } while (0);` AT THE END OF ONE `switch` ARM SHIFTS REORG'S BLOCK NUMBERING SO REORG DUPLICATES A LOOP-TEST `andi` INTO THE DISPATCH `beqz` DELAY SLOT - +1 INSTRUCTION, AND IT IS WHAT RETAIL SHOWS WHEN THE DISPATCHER IS ONE INSTRUCTION LONGER THAN YOURS** (round 420, menu/main/misc9 func_801DBE54, 93.91 -> 94.17% and 888 -> 892 bytes against retail's 896). Retail's do-while menu dispatcher is `sltiu v0,v1,11 / beqz v0,<loop test+4> / andi v0,s6,0xFF` (the loop test's FIRST instruction duplicated into the slot) with `sll v0,v1,2` as an ordinary instruction after the branch; every spelling of the switch gave reorg `sll v0,v1,2` for that slot instead, one instruction short, which three earlier rounds had recorded as an unreachable reorg decision. Adding `do { } while (0);` after the last statement of ONE arm (`prevCol = 0xFF;` then the empty loop) makes reorg emit retail's duplicated `andi` with no other change to the body. It is a permutation guard, not semantics: it also swapped the two loop flags between $s5 and $s6, so check the rest of the prologue afterwards. A second empty do-while at the one remaining site (case 4's tail, where retail keeps an extra `j`) measured identical (94.17 / 892), so the barrier is per-site. The permuter found this in 3 minutes (base 1337 best 1103) among otherwise wrong edits - when a park is one instruction short in a DELAY SLOT, run the permuter on it even if the note says the residual is reorg, then port only the do-while half.


- id: c-flipped-operand-fills-the-load-nop | keywords: delay-slot, nop, load, operand, commuted, and, block, layout, branch
  **A COMMUTATIVE `&`/`|`'s OPERAND ORDER DECIDES WHICH SIDE IS LOADED FIRST, SO A `nop` IN A GLOBAL LOAD'S DELAY SLOT (PLUS A MISPLACED SHARED TAIL BLOCK) IS CURED BY PUTTING THE GLOBAL FIRST** (round 426, field/main/misc2 func_800726E8, 93.36 -> 100.00 at retail's exact 212 insns / 848 bytes, XenoFieldNoGp). Retail tests `dollyStop & g_FieldDirectionMasks[dir]` with the dolly byte loaded right after `lh sceneAngle.vy` (that load's delay slot) and the mask byte emitted after the index chain; the park's `g_FieldDirectionMasks[dir] & dollyStop` emits `lh / nop / ... / lbu mask / lui+lbu dolly`, one instruction more in each of the two button blocks (214 vs 212 bytes) and the shared `frames == 0` tail laid out at +0x168 instead of retail's +0x2b0. gcc's expand_operands evaluates the LEFT operand first and lets the result reuse the RIGHT operand's register, so retail's `and v0,v0,a0` (mask in the result register) means the mask was the source's RIGHT operand. Measured on the same body: swapping the four `&` operands alone gave 99.93 at 212 insns with only three branch targets left, and nesting the two button blocks INSIDE the outer dolly `if` (so the shared tail is the function's last block, which is what those targets encode) closed it. So when a diff is one extra instruction plus a `nop` per repeated block and the branch targets are all shifted while the instruction multiset matches, suspect a commutative operand you spelled the other way round before touching the allocator or the block order.

- id: c-a-call-second-argument-is-emitted-last | keywords: delay-slot, nop, load, jal, argument, expand_call, maspsx, unreachable
  **A CALL'S SECOND ARGUMENT LOAD IS EMITTED AFTER THE FIRST ARGUMENT'S WHOLE ADDRESS CHAIN, SO WHEN RETAIL HAS IT IN THE FIRST ARGUMENT'S TABLE-LOAD DELAY SLOT NO SOURCE ORDER REACHES IT** (round 432, field/main/misc8 func_8008110C, parked 98.13% at 1260 bytes / 315 insns against retail's 1252 / 313). Retail's `SpritePlayAnimation(g_FieldActors[i].pSpriteData, pActor->animationOverride)` site is `lui v0,%hi(g_FieldActors) / lw v0,%lo()(v0) / lh a1,0xEA(s0) / addu v0,s3,v0 / lw a0,4(v0) / jal`, i.e. the second argument's load sits between the table load and the index addu. Reading cc1's own output for the same call (build/src/<unit>.c.s, right above the `jal`) shows `lw $2,g_FieldActors / #nop / addu $2,$19,$2 / lw $4,4($2) / lh $5,234($16)`: expand_call's load_register_parameters walks the arguments a0-first, so the a1 load is emitted LAST and cc1 marks the load-delay hole with a bare `#nop` that maspsx expands; reorg does not steal past the insn that consumes the load. A pre-call local (`s16 anim = pActor->animationOverride;`) moves the lh BEFORE the `lui %hi(g_FieldActors)` that opens the call (source order is contiguous), and the two-statement `pSprite = g_FieldActors[i].pSpriteData; SpritePlayAnimation(pSprite, ...)` folds back to the identical 98.13 / 315 - so this residue is retail's assembler filling the slot, not C. Diagnose by reading cc1's `.s` for a `#nop` between the load and its use before trying spellings.

- id: c-a-volatile-store-blocks-the-branch-slot-only | keywords: delay-slot, load-delay, reorg, volatile, store, increment, latch, hoist
  **A `volatile` STORE STOPS REORG SINKING THE STORE INTO A `bnez` DELAY SLOT BUT DOES NOT STOP IT HOISTING THE LOOP INCREMENT INTO THE PRECEDING GLOBAL LOAD'S DELAY SLOT, so a 4-byte-short body whose last body statement is that store reaches retail's exact size (352) with the store in the WRONG place (95.17%)** (round 442, battle/main/misc1 func_80072938, XenoBattle260 gcc 2.6.0, see also g-source-local-holding-the-doubled-index for the same body at 95.62 / 348). Spelling measured: `*(volatile u8 *)&pPrim[g_BattlePrimStartIndex + prim].b0 = 0;` as the loop's last statement - the non-volatile park gives `lw v0,symbol / addiu s2,s2,1 (stolen) / ...chain... / slt / bnez / sb zero,6(v1) (stolen)`, the volatile one gives `... / slt / sb zero,6(v1) / bnez / nop` (retail's branch-slot nop and its size) but the increment is STILL in the load slot and the store is after the `slt` where retail has it before the increment. So the volatile lever buys the delay slot it is aimed at and nothing else: it is worth one run on a one-instruction-short park, and the load-delay-slot hoist is a separate wall. cc1's own `.s` (build/src/<unit>.c.s) shows the `addiu` already in the load slot before the assembler, so maspsx is not involved.

- id: c-a-second-loads-position-inside-a-block-is-not-source-order | keywords: delay-slot, nop, load, order, va_arg, flags, cse, local-alloc
  **WHEN THE ONLY RESIDUAL IS A `nop` IN ONE LOAD'S DELAY SLOT BECAUSE RETAIL PUTS TWO INDEPENDENT LOADS IN THE OTHER ORDER, FIVE SOURCE SPELLINGS COMPILE BYTE-IDENTICALLY: cc1 (no `-fschedule-insns` in the PSY-Q preset) has already emitted its own order, so plan the spelling hunt around a SHAPE change, not statement order** (round 448, slus_006.64/psyq/libc_sprintf Sprintf, parked 98.87% / 2124 bytes / 531 insns against retail 2140 / 535 - the whole body, both digit loops, all three pad loops and the copyOut tail are instruction-identical, and the missing four instructions are exactly the four arms whose vararg lands in `value`). Retail's 'd'/'i' arm is `lw v1,0x220(sp) / addiu v0,v1,4 / sw v0,0x220(sp) / lw a0,0(v1) / lw v1,0x210(sp) / nop / andi v0,v1,0x20`; this compile swaps the last two loads (`lw v1,0x210(sp)` before `lw a0,0(v0)`), so the andi's source is not the freshly loaded register and the nop disappears. The 'n' and 's' arms load the flags FIRST in retail too, so it is not a rule about the arm. Measured byte-identical (five scored runs, `build/src/<unit>.c.s` compared each time): `fmt.u.flags` copied into a `u32` local after the fetch; the same local read BEFORE the fetch; `va_arg` split by hand into `pArg = ap; ap = pArg + 4; value = *(s32*)pArg;`; a `(u32)` cast around `va_arg`; and `value` declared first among the locals. `permute.sh --minutes 4` returns PERMUTER_BETTER base=674 best=522 on a candidate that wraps the fetch in a redundant `if (fmt.u.flags)` and duplicates it - wrong-shaped, not ported. Compare the two loads' positions in cc1's `.s` before spending more than one spelling on this class.
- id: a-pointer-local-dead-in-a-region-must-go | keywords: regalloc, local-alloc, pointer, temp, dead, inline, base, permuter
  **A POINTER LOCAL THAT IS ASSIGNED THE BARE BASE AND THEN USED BY A LATER CLUSTER OF STORES KEEPS THAT BASE PSEUDO LIVE ACROSS THE CLUSTER AND COSTS A REGISTER: delete the assignment and write the base inline in each store's address and local-alloc reuses the register, for free** (round 448, battle/main/misc18 func_80097D5C, 99.52 -> 99.56%, instruction and byte count unchanged at retail's exact 752 / 3024, differing lines 86 -> 76). Here `pDst = (u8*)g_BattleCurWorkBlock;` fed four stores at 0x3F4/0x3F5/0x800/0x801 and then nothing, while two arms set `pDst` again for their own store; rewriting the four as `((u32*)((u8*)g_BattleCurWorkBlock + (snapOffset = i * 8)))[0x3F4] = ...` closed one whole mirror pair (the base was in `$v0` where retail has `$v1`). Permuter-found (run 2 of 3, 1049 iterations, base 330 -> 330-internal-365->330); the companion edit it found on run 1 is the opposite shape on ONE arm only - `pDst = (u8*)(pWorkBlock = g_BattleCurWorkBlock) + i * 8` in the characterId != 4 arm while the == 4 arm keeps the plain form - worth 99.51 -> 99.52. So when a park's diff has several store clusters off the same global base and one intermediate pointer serves more than one of them, split it per cluster before hunting priority quotients; three mirror pairs of the allocno class remained after this and a third permuter run (1021 iterations) was base=best.

## operand order of addu and arithmetic spelling
- id: c-offset-first-sum-cast-flips-a-local-array-slot | keywords: addu, operand, base-first, index-first, cast, local, array, stack
  **FOR A STACK-LOCAL ARRAY SLOT the plain subscript puts the BASE in the `addu`'s destination (`normals[idx].vx` -> `addu v1,a0,v1`) and a cast over an offset-first sum puts the OFFSET there (`((VECTOR*)(idx * sizeof(VECTOR) + (u32)normals))->vx` -> retail's `addu v1,v1,a0`)** (round 441, field/main/misc8 func_80080A74, Default preset, still parked: 83.17 -> 83.39 but 1228 -> 1224 bytes, i.e. it buys the operand order and loses an instruction elsewhere). Same family as `c-p-tag-cast-flips-a-u8-store-index-first`: the cast-with-the-offset-written-first is the spelling that moves the base off the destination, and for a LOCAL array it is the only one measured. Judge it on the byte count: on that body the +0.22% came with a shorter body, so the operand fix alone is not a win.
- id: c-p-tag-cast-flips-a-u8-store-index-first | keywords: addu, operand, base-first, index-first, cast, p_tag, byte, store, memory-base
  **FOR A BYTE STORE THROUGH `polys[renderContext]` WITH A BASE THAT COMES FROM MEMORY, WRITING THE STORE THROUGH A `P_TAG*` CAST IS WHAT PUTS THE OFFSET FIRST - the typed `POLY_FT4` field write gives base-first plus a load-delay `nop` per store** (round 422, menu/main/misc8_r2 func_801DA9A8, parked 98.08%; 12 such stores in one function, `-0.6%` and 3 nops from the spelling alone). Retail is `addu v0,v0,v1` (destination = the `renderContext * 0x28` temp, base second) with `sb s0,0x1004(v0)`; `g_Menu->pItemRows->primGroupStrings[0].polys[g_Menu->renderContext].r0 = 0x80;` emits the mirror `addu v1,v1,v0` and, for the `g_Menu->itemRowStrings[row]` group, `addu v0,v0,a2` + `addu a0,a0,v0` (g_Menu added last) with a `nop` in every load-delay slot. `((P_TAG*)&....polys[g_Menu->renderContext])->r0 = 0x80;` (the misc9 func_801DCE60 spelling) gives retail's `addu a0,s1,a0` (base+row<<7 formed right after the `lw g_Menu`, filling the delay slot) then `addu v0,v0,a0`, and keeps the per-statement `lui/lw g_Menu` reloads. Measured with `cc1_probe.sh menu/main/misc8_r2` on six spellings in one snippet: the typed field write, the flat `((POLY_FT4*)primGroupStrings)[ctx]` cast, `((u8*)base)[0x1004 + ctx*0x28]` and its `*(...)` twin are all offset-first; only the typed struct-field write is base-first. The `SetSemiTrans(&....polys[ctx], 0)` argument (a pointer, not a byte store) was already offset-first in both.
- id: c-pointer-from-memory-paren-sum-is-base-first | keywords: addu, operand, base-first, index-first, pointer, field, paren, volatile
  **`((u8*)ptrField + i)[K]` IS THE BASE-FIRST SPELLING WHEN THE BASE POINTER COMES FROM MEMORY (`g_Menu->unk35C`), where `((u8*)ptrField)[K + i]` is index-first - and a `volatile`-QUALIFIED POINTER LOCAL is what puts such a base pair in retail's stack slots** (round 421, menu/main/misc8 func_801D8644, parked 88.01% under XenoMenu260 at 1964 bytes / 491 insns against retail 1952 / 488; whole 488-instruction body written from the assembly, no twin). Probed with `tools/scripts/cc1_probe.sh menu/main/misc8` on a four-spelling snippet: `pos[0x32C0 + i] = v`, `(pos + i)[0x32C0] = v`, `pos[i + 0x32C0] = v` and an in-place `p += i; p[0x32C0] = v` - only `(pos + i)[0x32C0]` emits retail's `addu $2,$2,$4` (base first, destination = the base register); the other three emit `addu $2,$4,$2`. That is the opposite verdict to `a-addu-index-first-is-the-legitimiser`, which measured the same parenthesised form as index-first for a PLAIN `u8 *` GLOBAL, so the pointer-from-memory base is the distinguishing case (same class as the round-419 `g_BattleGraphicsWork->pPacketPrims` finding, one edit per site, 85.02 -> 85.69 here from ten sites). Companion: declaring the two stat pointers `u16* volatile pStat;` / `u16* volatile pSaved;` forces both into 8-byte stack slots that are RELOADED at every use, which is exactly retail's `sw v0,64(sp)` then `lw a2,64(sp)` per row, and buying that one allocation change moved the whole body 85.69 -> 88.01 with no other edit (a plain `u16* pStat` kept them in `$s0`/`$s1` and cost the function two accumulator registers). Do not ship the volatile - it is a measurement tool here - but it identifies "spilled pointer pair" as the allocation retail had.
- id: c-double-negation-works-on-a-mem-field-base-and-then-decides-the-switch | keywords: addu, operand, negate, base-first, in-place, switch, case, order
  **THE DOUBLE NEGATION IS NOT ONLY FOR A POINTER VARIABLE: `MEMFIELD - (-(idx * 0x28))` PUTS A STRUCT/POINTER-FIELD SUM IN PLACE TOO, AND WHAT REGISTER IT LANDS IN THEN DECIDES WHICH `switch` CASE ORDER THE BODY NEEDS** (round 419, battle/main/misc17, gcc 2.6.0/XenoBattle260). func_80093578 matched 100.00 first try and func_8009187C went 98.22 -> 99.89 at retail's exact 700 bytes from one edit in each: retail forms every `g_BattleGraphicsWork->pPacketPrims + idx * 0x28` row address in place (`addu v1,v1,v0`, base first, destination = the base register), and `pRec = g_BattleGraphicsWork->pPacketPrims - (-(((s32 *)g_BattlePrimStartIndex)[0] * 0x28));` reproduces it where the plain `prims + idx * 0x28` gives the mirrored `addu v0,v0,v1` (pRec $v0, palette value $v1). It is the same sum at the two palette stores only; the call argument keeps the inline `prims + (idx * 0x28 + 0x320)` form. Two companion facts from the same pair: (1) once pRec is in place, 9187C's `switch` wants its cases listed 0, 1, 2, default -- the 0, 2, default, 1 order its old park note demanded (98.22, switch instruction-identical but a `nop` in the first `beq` slot) becomes wrong, so a case order is not a property of the switch, it is a property of the body's register state; (2) the double negation does NOTHING on a sum of two INTEGER offsets (`off = (..) * 0x28 + arg0 * 0x5F0`): it folds away and compiles identical, so 9187C's remaining residual (that sum's destination is the slot-offset register, `addu v0,v1,v0`, against retail's `addu v1,v1,v0`) is not reachable this way, and a fourth permuter round on the 99.89 body is PERMUTER_NONE base=20 best=20 over 1435 iterations. Round 426 (battle/main/misc16_r2 func_800904A0, same preset/tables, 91.97 -> 100.00 at retail's exact 620 bytes / 155 insns) adds two companion facts. (1) THE INLINE `g_BattlePrimStartIndex[0]` IN THE CALL IS WHAT PUTS `li a1,K` / `li a2,K` BEFORE THE `la` OF THE PRIM INDEX: with a `s32 *pMode = &g_BattlePrimStartIndex;` statement the `lui/addiu` pair is emitted there, the join block starts with it, reorg cannot split the pair, so the two `beqz` and one `beq` delay slots stay `nop` and the body is 4 bytes long (91.97, 624) - writing the index inline in the call argument lets right-to-left argument expansion emit the constant args first and leaves the address pseudo for the pointer argument, giving retail's order and the three filled slots. (2) IT ALSO DECIDES `default:`: 800904A0's retail switch is the same three cases with the case-0 block separate and a `j`-to-join fall-through for values over 2, so it wants NO default, where 9187C's identical-looking switch wanted one - the case list follows the body's register state here too.
- id: c-a-folding-constant-needs-its-own-local | keywords: reassociate, constant, fold, addiu, subtract, clamp, operand, local
  **A CLAMP WHOSE CONSTANT gcc CAN FOLD INTO THE SURROUNDING SUM NEEDS THAT CONSTANT IN A LOCAL, and the sibling statements come back one per variable** (round 404, field/dialogue/text_box func_8009C5A8, the last 0.3% of a 468-instruction body). Retail's box clamp is `left = x - 8 - width * 2` / `left + 16 + width * 4` / `y + 8 + height * 14`; written with the literals cc1 reassociates them into `x - (width * 2 + 8)` and `y + (height * 14 + 8)` (97.10, five instructions off each clamp), and `i = 8; left = x - i - width * 2;` / `i = 0x10; ...` / `i = 8; ...` (the `i` local reused for the two margins) reproduces retail's separate `addiu` chain. Two further statement-splitting facts measured on the same body and worth reaching for before a park: `y = screenY - height * 14 - 0x24;` in one expression computes `height * 14` BEFORE loading `screenY`, so the load has to be its own statement (`projectedY = screenY; projectedY = projectedY - height * 14; y = projectedY - 0x24;`); and an "already open, bail out" tail written inline lands at the head of the function while the same statements behind a `goto` to a label at the very end land where retail has them, moving every branch target in the body.

- id: c-a-double-negated-offset-flips | keywords: addu, operand, base-first, index-first, negate, subtract, address, tie
  **A DOUBLE-NEGATED OFFSET (`p - -(i * sizeof(T))`) MAKES cc1 PUT THE BASE IN THE `addu`'s DESTINATION, WHICH IS THE ONE SPELLING THAT FLIPPED THIS PARK'S FOUR MIRRORED ADDRESSES** (round 405, menu/main/misc7_r2 func_801D5ED4, 99.32 -> 100.00 at retail's exact 704 bytes / 176 insns, XenoMenu260 = gcc 2.6.0). Retail forms every `primBase + renderContext * 0x28` row address as `addu v1,v1,v0` - base FIRST, destination = the base register - where `(u8*)base + ctx * 0x28`, the `[ctx]` subscript, a named pointer local, `base + ctx` pointer arithmetic, the `pBase = base; pBase += ctx * sizeof;` in-place form and a pointer-arithmetic cast ALL emit the mirrored `addu v0,v0,a1` (offset first, destination = the offset temp) with the base and context registers swapped as well, at all four sites. Writing the offset with a double negation (`*(u16*)((u8*)g_Menu->pGearWindowPrims - -(g_Menu->renderContext * sizeof(POLY_FT4)) + 0x66) = ...`) closed one site per edit and each edit is exactly one site, so the four were measured independently: 99.43 (the 0x66 store), 99.72 (both palette arms), 100.00 (the `x0`/`y0` pair). The pointer-arithmetic form `((POLY_FT4*)base + ctx)` measures the same 99.32 as the subscript, so it is the negation that carries the fix, and `sizeof(POLY_FT4)` at all four sites is code-identical to the literal 0x28. Do not reach for this on a body that is otherwise fine - the in-place `pBase +=` form is still the one to try for a `pBase + CONST` store - but for a base/offset pair that mirrors at EVERY site of one function, the negated offset is one edit per site, and the permuter does find it (this park's note recorded a 1392-iteration run with no find on the same body; a 1324-iteration run found exactly this edit, base 120 -> 100).

- id: c-the-negation-closes-a-window-prim-tail-and-a-typed-view-loses-it | keywords: addu, operand, base-first, index-first, negate, tail, store, poly_ft4, struct, block
  **THE DOUBLE NEGATION CONFIRMED ON THE SAME UNIT'S GEAR-WINDOW TAIL, AND THE MEASURED PROOF THAT A TYPED `POLY_FT4` / BLOCK VIEW OF THE SAME BYTES IS NOT THE FIX** (round 430, menu/main/misc8 func_801D7CFC, 97.08 -> MATCH at retail's exact 596 bytes / 149 insns under XenoMenu260). The body was parked at 97.08 with a whole-tail instruction-order residual (retail loads `pGearWindowPrims` two slots earlier and forms `addu a0,a0,v1` base-first for the `tpage |= 0x20` read-modify-write, index-first for the three `r0`/`g0`/`b0` byte stores); ONE edit - `*(u16*)((u8*)g_Menu->pGearWindowPrims - -((g_Menu->renderContext + selected * 2) * sizeof(POLY_FT4)) + 0x1E16) |= 0x20;` - closed the whole tail at once (the three byte stores kept their plain index-first casts; they already matched). The natural-looking typed spelling of the SAME statements (`typedef struct { u8 unk0[0x1E00]; POLY_FT4 windowPrims[2][2]; SVECTOR windowQuadVertices[2][4]; ... }` with `pBlock->windowPrims[sel][ctx].tpage |= 0x20` / `->r0 = 0x20` / `->x0`) measures 64.37% at 592 bytes / 148 insns, because the block pointer becomes a loop-invariant local that cc1 hoists and the whole tail then shares one register. So: when the loop already matches through `pBase`/`pEntry` offset locals, do not "clean up" the tail into a struct view to match the identical bytes - measure it, expect ~64%, and keep the negated cast.

- id: c-the-negation-does-not-survive-a-loop-index | keywords: addu, operand, base-first, index-first, negate, giv, loop, accumulator, tie
  **THE DOUBLE NEGATION FLIPS AN OFFSET THAT IS A MULTIPLY OF A NON-LOOP REGISTER ONLY: WHEN THE OFFSET IS THE LOOP'S OWN `i * STRIDE`, loop.c STRENGTH-REDUCES THE NEGATED FORM INTO A NEW ACCUMULATOR WITH A NEGATIVE STEP AND THE BODY COSTS AN EXTRA INSTRUCTION** (round 406, menu/main/misc9 func_801DD5E8, still parked 99.72% at retail's exact 424 bytes / 106 insns, gcc 2.6.0). The two residuals are the addresses of two `lbu renderContext` loads, `panel + i * 0x80` with `panel = g_Menu->unk430` loaded at the use site; retail writes both base-first (`addu v0,v1,s0`, `addu v0,v0,v1`) and cc1 2.6.0 offset-first (`addu v0,s0,v1`, `addu v1,v1,v0`), same operand pair, same instruction multiset. The round-405 win's spelling applied here (`(u8*)g_Menu->unk430 - -(i * sizeof(MenuString)) + 0xF7D`) does put the base first, but as `subu v0,v1,s3` off a fresh negative accumulator (`addiu s3,s3,-128` in the latch), so it is 92.78% at the same 424 bytes with the register set rotated. `cc1_probe.sh` on a standalone copy of the loop (base-first cast, offset written first, a base pointer local assigned inside the loop, an embedded assignment temp `(pBase = (u8*)g_Menu->unk430)`, an index-first struct view, a u16 index) emits `addu $2,$2,$3` - offset first - for every one, and a fourth `permute.sh -j 2` run (1189 iterations, base=30 best=30) ends at the same body. So a park whose only residual is a base/offset `addu` order where the offset is a loop index's own multiply is the legitimiser, not the source: park it, do not respell.

- id: c-struct-member-array-subscript-flips-a-loop-index-addu | keywords: addu, operand, base-first, index-first, struct, member, array, loop, subscript
  **AN `addu` WHOSE OFFSET IS THE LOOP'S OWN `i * STRIDE` DOES FLIP TO RETAIL'S BASE-FIRST ORDER WHEN THE ARRAY IS A STRUCT MEMBER AND THE INDEX IS THE BARE LOOP VARIABLE - what cannot flip it is any cast spellings of the same address, including `(T*)field[i + K]`, `((u8*)field + i * N)[K]` and the paren sum, which all cse to index-first** (round 443, menu/main/misc9 func_801DD5E8, 99.72 -> MATCH at retail's exact 424 bytes / 106 insns, XenoMenu260 = gcc 2.6.0; this CORRECTS `c-the-negation-does-not-survive-a-loop-index`, which ends "park it, do not respell"). The two residuals were the `lbu renderContext` addresses of the 0x430 panel's two shifted sprite groups: retail `addu v0,v1,s0` / `addu v0,v0,v1` (base first, displacement 0x77D / 0xF7D), every cast spelling `addu v0,s0,v1`. The panel is a `void*` (`g_Menu->unk430`) so the twin's own spelling was unavailable as written. Fix: declare a file-local view struct with the two record arrays as members at their real offsets (`u8 unk0[0x700]; MenuString shadedRowStrings[0xE]; MenuString columnStrings[2]; MenuString headingStrings[2];`, the same idiom as this file's existing `MenuUnk430ValueStrings`) and write `->shadedRowStrings[i].renderContext`. The struct-member form gives the address the MEM(base, index=i, scale=0x80, disp=0x77D) shape whose index is the biv, so cc1 emits `sll vN,i,0x7` + base-first `addu`; the cast form folds the constant into the pointer and the legitimiser then prefers index-first. Two companion measurements on the same body: (a) `->valueStrings[i]` (the neighbouring 0x700 array of the existing view struct) works identically, and (b) an index that is a SUM (`->valueStrings[0x10 + i]`) does NOT - loop.c strength-reduces it into a second running accumulator (`li s3,0x800` / `addiu s3,s3,0x80`) and the whole body rotates to 94.14% at the same 424 bytes, so the constant base must be the member's, not the index's. Also measured dead here: taking the row entry as a typed `MenuString* pString` used for BOTH the `polys[rc].r0` gate and the call address (97.74% / 416 bytes - retail forms the gate's address as `base + (i*0x80 + rc*0x28)` and the call's as `(base + i*0x80) + rc*0x28`, two different trees, so the gate must stay a separate expression).
- id: c-a-minus-chain-reassociates-the-subtract | keywords: reassociate, subtract, subu, operand, order, crossjump, tail, local, clamp
  **`(C - a) - b` IS REASSOCIATED TO `(C - b) - a`, WHICH SWAPS WHICH VALUE GOES IN WHICH REGISTER AND - WHEN TWO ARMS SHARE THE TAIL - COSTS THE CROSS-JUMP; a `v = C - a; return v - b;` LOCAL PUTS RETAIL'S ORDER BACK, BUT gcc THEN MERGES ONLY THE LAST `subu`** (round 411, slus_006.64/psyq/libgpu_b func_80045C94, parked 92.73% at 184 bytes against retail's 176 / 44 insns). Retail holds `0x400 - w - p->x` in two arms and executes it once: case 2 jumps INTO case 1's tail at +0x40 with `li v0,1024` in the delay slot. Five spellings measured: the plain `return 0x400 - p->w - p->x` in both arms 88.02 (v1 = **x**, a0 = w - the loads come out `lh v1,0` / `lh a0,4`, mirrored, and no merge); the same with `w = p->w / 2;` in case 2 92.68; `v = 0x400 - p->w; return v - p->x;` in both arms 87.16 (retail's operand order and load order, 45 insns, but both arms jump to a THIRD block holding `subu v0,v0,a0` instead of case 2 jumping into case 1); the v-form in case 1 plus the w-local in case 2 92.73 - the park. A 4-minute permuter run (base 457 -> 317) found only the un-parenthesised case-2 arm, which is 88.07 on objdiff, so its metric and objdiff disagree here: judge this class by the block layout, not the score. Companion fact from the sibling clamp in the same unit (func_80045A34, 79.12% at 188 bytes against retail's 204): retail spends TWO extra instructions per axis on a `move a2,v0` that copies the loaded `g_GpuScreenWidth` plus a `addiu v0,a2,-1` that RECOMPUTES `G - 1` in the clamp block, i.e. its build did not cse that expression across the branch, while cc1 here computes `addiu v1,v0,-1` once and moves it into the merge; the if/else clamp-block spelling that should separate the two uses scores 65.59, so an "extra instructions retail has" gap of exactly `1 copy + 1 recompute` per bounded axis is a cse difference to park, not a source shape to find.

- id: d-array-extern-hoists-its-address-scalar-extern-does-not | keywords: addu, operand, in-place, array, scalar, extern, hoist, cse
  **THE OBSTACLE TO THE IN-PLACE `+=` FORM FOR AN ARRAY-BASED GLOBAL IS THE ARRAY, NOT THE CONSTANT ADDRESS: with `extern u8 *g[2]` cc1 hoists `la t0,g` out of the loop and reloads through it, while the SAME global declared as the SCALAR `extern u8 *g` keeps the per-site symbolic `lw v0,g` and then gives retail's base-first `addu`** (round 418, battle/main/misc23 func_800A5EB4, 92.95 -> 99.16 at 1412 bytes / 353 insns against retail's 1424 / 356; the previous park's 99.58 body had retail's exact size but all 15 `g_BattlePartPrimBuffers` operand orders mirrored). The probe (`cc1_probe.sh battle/main/misc23`) settles it in 10 s: the identical loop body over `extern void *gArr[]` emits `la $7,gArr` once + `lw $2,0($7)` per site, over `extern void *gScalar` it emits `lw $2,gScalar` per site - and both give base-first `addu $2,$2,$3` for the two-statement form (`p = g; p += i * 0x18; pDst[0] = p[0x284];`). So when a TU uses only element 0 of a global that another unit declares as an array (`extern u8 *g[2]` in misc22), declaring it scalar in this TU is what puts the address back inside the MEM. Casts do not substitute: `*(u8 **)&g` re-creates the array address (hoist), and `*(u8 * volatile *)&g` forces an INDIRECT address (`lui/addiu/lw`, one instruction more than retail's `lui/lw`).

- id: c-two-or-terms-need-a-local | keywords: or, operand, order, reassociate, fold, constant, local, pack
  **A WORD THAT IS `(value << N) | CONST | (mem & mask)` NEEDS THE `(value << N) | CONST` HALF IN ITS OWN LOCAL, or cc1 moves the constant next to the memory operand and the final `or`'s destination swaps** (round 421, slus_006.64/psyq/libgpu_b func_80045E44, 98.51 -> MATCH at retail's exact 151 insns / 604 bytes, PSY-Q preset). Retail builds the E1 draw-mode word as `srl/sll` (color >> 31 << 10) / `or v0,v0,v1` (0xE1000000) / ... / `andi a0,a0,0x7ff` / `or a0,a0,v0` - the result in the register the *status* read was in. Written inline as `(color >> 31 << 10 | 0xE1000000) | (*g_GpuGp1Port & 0x7FF)` cc1's fold reassociates the constant onto the memory term and emits `or a0,a0,v1` then `or v0,v0,a0` (96.03%); moving the whole `(color >> 31 << 10) | 0xE1000000` subexpression *out of the arms* to one local before the `if` is worse still (87.70, the result wanted `$t2` across both arms and the body lost 4 instructions). The closed form is a local declared at the TOP of each arm (`u_long drawMode; drawMode = (color >> 31 << 10) | 0xE1000000;`) - one per arm, not shared, because retail reloads the `lui 0xE1000000` in each arm - plus the memory term FIRST in the final `or` (`(*g_GpuGp1Port & 0x7FF) | drawMode`); the operand order there is what makes the destination retail's register rather than a mirror. Companion fact: `limitRange(x, 0, W - 1)` from libgpu.h is the exact clamp retail has on both axes of this body, but its `move a2,v0` + recomputed `addiu v0,a2,-1` per axis is the same cse gap already recorded under c-a-minus-chain-reassociates-the-subtract - here gcc cse'd it and the prologue still matched, so do not chase it.

- id: i-if-else-inside-a-loop-canonicalises | keywords: if, else, block, order, layout, arm, break, continue, canonicalise, loop
  **AN IF/ELSE WHOSE TWO ARMS BOTH END IN `break`/`continue` INSIDE ONE LOOP IS CANONICALISED BY cc1 2.6.0, SO THE ARM ORDER YOU WRITE CANNOT MOVE THE BLOCK LAYOUT - measure it once, then park** (round 430, menu/main/misc8 func_801D9704, 84.26% at retail's exact 260 bytes / 65 insns under XenoMenu260). Retail is `bnez a2 -> gear block` with the `currentCharacterIDs` arm as the fall-through and the gear block placed after it, just before the return; cc1 emits the mirror (`beqz a2 -> characterIDs`, gear arm fall-through) and, worse, `addiu a0,a0,1` before the case's `andi a2,a2,0xff`/`lui g_Menu` group where retail has it last. Four spellings measured and all byte-identical to each other: the if/else-if, an else-less then-arm ending in an explicit `continue` (the shape retail's duplicated `addiu a0,a0,1` in BOTH back-edge delay slots suggests), the same with the arms swapped, and `if (!arg2)`. Earlier rounds on the same body also measured `while (1)` + trailing `arg0++`, a `pManager` local (66.43), and `goto` labels (right arm order, 62.38 - the loop stops being recognised and `g_Menu`/the 0xFF constant stop being hoisted) plus an explicit `arg0++; continue;` with a second `arg0++` at the bottom (85.62, cross-jumped increments, +8 bytes). Block order is not a source shape when the arms are symmetric: park it.

- id: c-base-plus-field-offset-is-canonicalised | keywords: addu, operand, offset, field, pointer, local, subscript, canonicalise, probe
  **`(u8*)pBase + pBase->fieldOffset` IS CANONICALISED BASE-FIRST BY gcc 2.7.2 FOR ALL SIX SPELLINGS, INCLUDING A DECLARED `s32` OFFSET LOCAL - the operand order follows the pseudo numbering of base vs load temp, not the source, so a park whose only residual is the mirror of retail's `addu v0,v0,v1` is closed** (round 441, battling/main/misc10 func_80084C88, parked 97.37% at retail's exact 840 bytes / 210 insns; retail has the offset as the addu's first operand AND destination where the body emits base-first with the same destination). Probed with `cc1_probe.sh` on a snippet reproducing the function's real six-statement context (`pScript` / `pPartTimings` / `pModelScriptOffsets` / `pActions` stores around the one in question): the plain sum, the offset written first (`pHeader->offset + (u8*)pHeader`), an `(s32)` cast, the subscript form `&((u8*)pHeader)[off]`, and a named `s32` offset declared before AND after `pHeader` all emit the identical `lw $2,48($3)` / `addu $3,$3,$2` (base first, destination = the first operand, i.e. the base; the load temp folds into the sum so the declared local changes nothing). Read this with `b-commutative-sum-will-canonicalise`, where a copy of a PARAMETER does move the pair: that trick needs the copied value to stay a live pseudo, and a memory load's result cannot. The 2.6.0 route is also measured dead here - that compiler keeps source order, so the offset-first spelling was scored on the whole body: 96.13, the same number as the plain spelling, i.e. the addu leg buys a 2.6.0 switch nothing (cdk 94.04, Default 97.37).

- id: c-a-base-pointer-local-expands-the-address-before-the-index | keywords: addu, address, order, prologue, lui, addiu, sll, base, index, local
  **WHEN RETAIL'S PROLOGUE EXPANDS THE BASE ADDRESS (`lui`/`addiu`) BEFORE THE INDEX MULTIPLY AND EVERY SINGLE-EXPRESSION SPELLING EMITS THE INDEX FIRST, ONE `T* p = base;` STATEMENT ABOVE THE SUBSCRIPT SWAPS THE TWO GROUPS AND IS BYTE-NEUTRAL** (round 448, menu/main/misc8_r2 func_801DA9A8, 99.04 -> MATCH at retail's exact 1668 bytes / 417 insns, XenoMenu260 = gcc-2.6.0). For `pItemId = &g_GameState.itemIDs[arg1 * 2 + arg0];` retail is `lui v0,%hi(g_GameState+0x2026)` / `addiu v0,v0,%lo(...)` then `sll a1,a1,0x1` / `addu a1,a1,s1` / `addu s3,a1,v0`; the subscript, the `g_GameState.itemIDs + idx` pointer form and the swapped index order all emit the `sll`/`addu` pair first (99.04, 99.48, 99.04, each 4 differing lines and retail's byte count), and `pItemIds = g_GameState.itemIDs; pItemId = &pItemIds[arg1 * 2 + arg0];` gives retail's order exactly. The pointer base survives as its own pseudo because it is a declared local, so expand_expr has an already-materialised base to emit before the index; a cast or a folded symbol does not (same mechanism as several `cast` levers above). Count it as a spelling, not a scope change: the local is dead after the one statement, costs no register and no instruction.

## extra or missing move

symptoms: one extra or missing move / second copy of a value
try: move copy missing extra
- id: a-preheader-copy-needs-the-earlier-loop-scratch | keywords: move, copy, coalesce, preheader, callee-saved, loop, counter, scratch
  **A `move dst,src` AT A LOOP PREHEADER THAT A SECOND LOCAL LOSES (cc1 coalesces the src/dst pair because src dies there) IS REACHED BY MAKING THE DESTINATION THE *SAME* VARIABLE AN EARLIER LOOP ALREADY USED AS ITS COUNTER - one variable, two roles, and the whole callee-saved hand-out follows** (round 438, world_map/main/misc1 func_80072DB4, 96.65 -> 97.81 -> 99.94% at retail's exact 1356 bytes / 339 insns, XenoWorldMap). Retail is `move s5,a1` (colour param) then `addu s2,s5,zero` in the entry test's delay slot, and `s2` is ALSO the FT4 column-init loop's counter at the top of the function - so the colour's pseudo is one whose live range already crosses the four HeapAlloc/GetTPage calls, which is exactly why it is callee-saved and why the copy survives; a separate `nColor` gets `$s3` (the freed param register), coalesces with `color` into one pseudo and the copy disappears, 1352 bytes and unlinkable. Two more load-bearing shapes on the same body, each worth its own instructions: the count-down loop is written `count--; for (; count != -1; count--)` (a single pre-decrement statement, so loop.c cannot fold it to `beqz count`, and the parameter register is reused IN PLACE as `addiu s7,s7,-1` - `for (i = count - 1; i != -1; i--)` and a `nEnd = -1` local both cost a register and 4-8 bytes of frame), and retail's third-column `setUV4` has u3 = u1 = the column width (0x40), not the first column's 0x80 - that one value error alone kept 12 store rows permuted AND was a wrong byte in the overlay, so read every setUV4 argument off the `sb` list before blaming sched2 for a store-order diff. Residual after all three: only the two `PutDispEnv`/`PutDrawEnv` pair relocations name `D_8009BC9C` in retail where the typed `g_WorldMapGfxEnvs[1].dispEnv` gives base+0xD4 (same address, check_bin OK) - and the `(DRAWENV*)((u8*)pDispEnv - 0x5C)` cast is load-bearing: `pEnv = &g_WorldMapGfxEnvs[1]; PutDispEnv(&pEnv->dispEnv); PutDrawEnv(&pEnv->drawEnv);` is 98.76%.
- id: b-cse-reuses-a-limit-behind-a-branch | keywords: move, copy, cse, limit, addiu, clamp, regenerate, branch
  **A CLAMP LIMIT RETAIL COMPUTES TWICE FROM A PRESERVED COPY OF THE LOADED BASE (`addu a2,v0,zero` / `addiu v0,v0,-1` for the compare, `addiu v0,a2,-1` in the arm) IS NOT REACHABLE FROM SOURCE WHEN IT IS A CHEAP reg+const EXPRESSION: gcc 2.7.2 cse1 keeps ONE pseudo and copies it into the arm (`addiu v1,v0,-1` / `move a0,v1`), and 14 spellings that name the limit twice still merge** (round 424, slus_006.64/psyq/libgpu_b func_80045A34, parked 79.12% at 188 bytes against retail's 204 - 4 instructions, 2 per axis, so it cannot link; the sibling func_80045B00 is the same body plus an opcode word and parks the same way at 79.80%). Measured with `cc1_probe.sh` on this unit's flags, every one giving a single `-1` and a move into the arm: ternary on the parameter, if/else-if with and without an else arm, a limitRange-style macro on a local, the same macro with the limit textually duplicated, an s16 mirror local, `int`/`u_short` locals holding W-1, comparing against `>= g_GpuScreenWidth` (which instead computes the limit AFTER the slt and costs the same 1), `int w = g_GpuScreenWidth;` with `w - 1` written twice, sequential ifs, and m2c's hoisted-limit shape. Presets ruled out: 2.7.2-cdk 75.78, 2.6.0 79.12.
