Load-bearing in the parked body: `u_long t[4]` reproduces retail's 0x10 frame with no store anywhere (`.frame $sp,16`, so it goes in the bltz delay slot), and the mode test spelled `!= 1 && != 2` gives retail's `sltiu` + bnez.
- id: b-increment-hoisted-above-a-call-pair | keywords: move, copy, increment, addiu, delay, reorg, loop, counter
  **A LOOP COUNTER RETAIL INCREMENTS IN TWO INSTRUCTIONS (`addiu $vN,counter,1` then `move counter,$vN`) IS INCREMENTED WITH THE SUM HOISTED ABOVE THE PRECEDING CALL PAIR AND THE COPY BELOW IT - the one-instruction spellings all coalesce and reorg then steals the `addiu` into the previous `jal`'s delay slot, where retail has a `nop`** (round 409, field/main/misc3 FieldFree, 98.66% at 652 bytes against retail's 656 -> 100.00 / 656). Retail is `jal GfxFlushImageList / nop / jal DrawSync / move a0,zero / addiu v0,s1,1 / lui+lw g_FieldCurRenderContextIndex / move s1,v0 / addu a0,a0,s1` with the loop test at the bottom. What reproduces it is `do { next = i + 1; GfxFlushImageList(); DrawSync(0); i = next; GfxSetRenderContext((g_FieldCurRenderContextIndex + i) & 1); ... } while (i < 2);` - the sum lands in `$v0` before the two calls, the copy into the counter `$s1` after them, and the pair is then too far from any branch to be target-filled. Measured dead ends, all 98.66 / 652: `i++`, `++i`, `i += 1`, `i = i + 1`, `(i = i + 1)` in the argument and `next = i + 1; i = next;` AT the increment site (after `DrawSync`, before the context call) - every one coalesces to a single `addiu`; `i++` at the loop top; using `next` instead of `i` in the context argument; gcc-2.7.2-cdk 90.87 and -O3 98.66. A permuter run found it from a two-temp body (`new_var = i + 1` at the top, `next = new_var; i = next;`) in 67 iterations; the single temp with the copy after the call pair is the tidy equivalent and also scores 0.
- id: d-mult-by-2-emits-a-copy | keywords: move, copy, sll, mult, shift, expand_binop, park
  **`x * 2` whose result has to live in a callee-saved register costs an extra `move`; `x << 1` does not** (round 394, battle/misc23 func_800A9540, 376 -> retail's 372 bytes, 94.49 -> 94.73). cc1's expand of `index = slotTextIndex * 2;` emits a TEMP plus a copy (`sll v1,v1,1` / `move s0,v1`, confirmed in the -dg dump as a `(set (reg 100) (ashift (reg 74) 1))` followed by `(set (reg 76) (reg 100))` in the pre-alloc .combine dump, i.e. it is in the RTL, not the allocator), while `index = slotTextIndex << 1;` expands the shift straight into index's allocno (`sll s0,v1,1`) and reaches retail's byte count. When a body is exactly one `move` too long and the value is a multiply by two, write the shift.
- id: d-repeated-base-i-stride | keywords: move, copy, loop, addu, cc1, delay, permuter, pointer
  **A repeated `base + i * stride` sum that retail COPIES into a second register is source-controllable: give the LOOP its own pointer local.** round 370, battle/misc9 (a permuter find, base 974 -> 263): `pRow = (u16 *)((arg1 & 0xFF) * 0x40 + (s32)g_BattleSlotWordRegs); pRow[idx] = 0; pField = pRow;` and then the loop reads `pField` - the sum pseudo stays live past the copy, so cc1 emits retail's `addu v1,v1,v0` + `move s5,v1` instead of coalescing both uses into `addu s5,v1,a0`. The same body then parked ONE instruction short (93.64%, 344 vs retail's 348) because retail has the `i++` in the beqz delay slot after a call while reorg target-fills the loop test there; that residual is not reachable from source, so park it with the number.
- id: d-live-value-costs-nothing | keywords: move, copy, s32, addiu, addu, li, loop, lw
  **A live-in value that costs nothing is a PARAMETER** (round 335): retail's `lw v0,sym / addu a1,v0,zero / addiu v1,a1,1` copy appears when the local is LIVE AT THE LOAD, and the free spelling is that the value IS one of the function's parameters (the caller already cleared that register). A local initialised to -1 gives the same loop but costs `li a1,-1` (404 vs retail's 400 bytes). system/archive func_8002B8B0 matched at 100.00 as `f(s32 param0, s32 nQueueIndex)`.
  Round 418 counter-measurement in the same unit, for the case where the index has to SURVIVE A CALL: func_8002B5D0/func_8002B2F0 want retail's `move s2,v0 / addiu v1,s2,1` in a scan loop whose index is live across `CdGetSector`. Making it the second parameter produces the copy in the right order but MIRRORS the callee-saved pair (the index takes `$s1`, the in-loop pointer `$s2`, retail has the opposite) and grows the prologue by one `sw` - 96.71% / 740 bytes / 185 insns against the local's 97.47 / retail's exact 736. So the trick pays when the parameter's register is the one retail uses (a caller-saved `$aN`); when the variable is callee-saved, its cold pseudo at function entry wins the FIRST free saved register and every other pointer in the function shifts.
- id: d-sltiu-copy-idiom-retail | keywords: move, copy, sltiu, u8, addu, delay, s32
  THE SLTIU-COPY IDIOM: when retail does 'sltiu v0,<x>,1' then 'addu t0,v0,zero' in the 'j' delay slot instead of 'sltiu t0,<x>,1', the boolean local must be u8, not u32/s32 - a u8 return makes gcc compute the compare into v0 and copy it into the return register (two functions first try in battle/misc12 after the u32 versions sat at 95.5%).
- id: d-leftover-a1-a2-jal | keywords: move, copy, delay, jal
  A leftover `$a1`/`$a2` at a `jal` usually means the source passes a COMPUTED value there and the parked C replaced it with a literal: check every call site's delay slot before blaming register allocation (round 47, battling/misc10 func_80084B48: 95.98 -> 100%).
- id: d-two-0xff-immediates-cse | keywords: move, copy, cse, s32
  Two 0xFF immediates are CSE'd into one register unless they differ in mode: give one of them an 's32 v = 0xFF;' local.
- id: d-clamp-copy-shape-round | keywords: move, copy, slti, u8, addu, s32
  THE CLAMP-COPY SHAPE (round 35, took two stubs from 93.3/94.5 to 100.00 first try): when retail COPIES the result to a second register before the compare ('addu v1,v0,zero; slti v1,v1,0x100'), it had a separate narrow variable - write 's32 value = <sum>; u8 result = value; if (value > 0xFF) result = 0xFF; pTbl[k] = result;'. A single variable gives a test-in-place; the 'u8' copy pseudo is what reproduces retail's extra move. Try this before calling any 'retail copies X to $vN before bgez/slti' diff an allocator tie.
- id: d-second-walker-must-be | keywords: move, copy, pointer, loop, param, regalloc, twin
  **A SECOND WALKER must be a local initialised at ENTRY from the parameter, and the first loop walks the PARAMETER** (round 389, battle/misc21 func_8009F1C4 96.45 -> 100.00). Retail keeps two copies of the array base across the whole function (`move s3,a0` for the first loop's walker, `move s6,s3` for the copy the LAST loop restarts from); writing `pSprite = pSprites;` as a re-assignment before the last loop instead of `BattleSprite* pSprite = pSprites;` at the top costs an extra `move` at that point, puts the wrong copy in `$s6` and permutes every register in the function (249 vs 251 insns, 94.26). Read the twin's own source for which name walks which loop: src/battle/main/misc21's matched neighbour BattleUpdateSpriteTransforms has the same pair.
- id: d-one-instruction-short-park | keywords: move, copy, loop, lhu, u16, volatile, lbu, lui
  AN "ONE INSTRUCTION SHORT" PARK IS A MISSING LOAD, NOT AN ALLOCATOR PROBLEM (round 299, battle/misc15 func_8008ADD0: 98.54 percent at 820 bytes against retail's 824 - it could never link). The missing instruction was a reload: retail re-forms lui %hi + lhu %lo(sym) on EVERY loop iteration while any spelling that also keeps the symbol's address in a register reads through the saved register. What restores it is *(volatile u16 *)&g_Sym inside the loop body, and the saved register the same change frees comes from new_var = &g_Sym; placed BEFORE the branch with pMask = new_var; at the use. That body is now 99.90 percent at retail's exact 824 bytes. So when you are 4 bytes short, look for the load retail has that you do not - a lbu/lhu/lw of a global inside a loop is the usual one - and try the volatile-u16 read before anything else.

- id: d-loop-bottom-accumulator-copy | keywords: move, copy, loop, or, delay, coalesce, park
  **An accumulator update at a loop bottom whose retail body copies THROUGH A TEMP is not reachable by spelling: cc1 coalesces the two live-out variables into one register** (round 392, battle/main/misc10 func_8007F8C0, parked 99.30% at 684 bytes against retail's 688, one instruction short and so never linkable). Retail's tail is `or v1,s3,s0` / `move s3,v1` (in the beqz delay slot) / `move s0,s3` / `andi v0,s0,0xff`, i.e. the OR lands in a scratch that is copied to the accumulator, and the accumulator then to the returned variable; every source shape that writes `next = acc | ret; acc = next; ret = acc;` compiles to `or s0,s3,s0` / `move s3,s0` because acc and ret hold equal values and gcc gives them one register. Measured dead ends: the copies after the loop instead of inside it (93.49, `or s3,s3,s0` in the beqz slot, 684), `ret = next` with the exit reading the other variable (99.30, byte-identical to the coalesced body), chained assignment, u8 opcode local, for(;;)+break, two inline returns, and three permuter runs from the 99.30 body (1479/1225/995 iterations, all RESULT PERMUTER_NONE). Round 405 adds two more: the temp kept in the loop with the copy moved BELOW it (`next = acc | ret; acc = next;` then `ret = acc;` after the loop) is 93.49% / 684 bytes - cc1 folds next into acc (`or s3,s3,s0`) AND folds the exit copy into the return (`andi v0,s3,0xff`), losing two copies; and a fourth permuter run from the 99.30 body (1502 iterations, base=111 best=111, PERMUTER_NONE). Preset-independent: 99.3 under every 2.7.2 -G4/-G8 combination, 94.59 under 2.7.2-cdk -O2.  Round 419 improves the park to **99.80% at retail's exact 688 bytes / 172 insns** (was 99.30 / 684, one short): the two loop-bottom copies DO come back once the temp is LIVE AT A MERGE - `if (!(cont & 0xFF)) goto out;` ... `next = ret; next = acc | next; acc = next;` ... `ret = next;` `out:` `return ret & 0xFF;` - and only `char ret` (or `u8 ret`) keeps cc1 from folding it, which is also what buys retail's schedule (the OR stays after the `lw`, reorg fills the beqz slot from the exit block). Residual then is 3 rows of one cause: the OR's second operand is the zero-extended copy of `ret` in a scratch (`or v1,v1,s3`, exit `move s0,v1`, delay-slot andi in v1) instead of ret's own register (`or v1,s3,s0`, `move s0,s3`). A plain `s32 ret` with the same goto merge keeps the temp but reorg steals the OR into the beqz delay slot (99.30 / 684). Do not re-brief this residual as a hand job. Round 431 measured the shape that drops the shared label entirely - `if (!(cont & 0xFF)) return ret & 0xFF;` inside the loop plus `return ret & 0xFF;` after it - and it DOES give retail's exit-block `andi` in the cont beqz delay slot with the OR after the `lw`, so that half of the residual is reachable and the label is not needed: `char ret` 99.16 / 684, `s32 ret` 99.30 / 684 (there the OR is raw, `or v1,s3,s0` exactly as retail, and the missing instruction is the folded acc/ret pair: `or s0,s3,s0` + `move s3,s0`), `next = acc|ret / acc = next / ret = next` with the temp 96.95 / 684 (reorg moves the OR into the delay slot and sinks the early `andi` to the shared exit). An empty `do { } while (0);` after case 0x99's `cont = 1` on the two-return body measures identical to 96.95 / 684, so the c-an-empty-do-while-in-a-case barrier does not shift reorg in a 28-case dispatcher; a sixth permuter run from the 99.80 body (1773 iterations, base 21 best 21, 3 minutes) finds nothing.
- id: d-select-two-assignments-if-else | keywords: move, copy, j, bgez, diamond, select, ternary, branch
  **A CLAMP-TO-ZERO SELECT WHOSE RETAIL ARM IS `j merge` IS AN IF/ELSE WITH TWO ASSIGNMENTS, NOT A TERNARY, AND NOT A `y = 0;` HOIST.** (round 394, slus_006.64/system/temp2 func_800273C4 - parked at 99.02%, 12 bytes / 3 instructions short, for two rounds; the if/else closes it to 100.00 at retail's exact 1332 bytes / 333 instructions). Retail's diamond is `lh v0,0x22(sp); nop; bgez v0,Lelse; move v1,v0; [then] move a0,zero; j Lmerge; [Lelse] move a0,v1; Lmerge: sll v0,a0,16; sra v0,v0,16` - branch on the negated condition, the value SPECULATED into that branch's delay slot, two separate assignments to the same destination, and a `j` over the taken arm. Writing `y = (proj2.vy < 0) ? 0 : proj2.vy;` gives a REGISTER-ONLY select (`bgez; move a0,v0; move v0,zero; move a0,v0`) with no `j` and drops the destination's stack slot; and the park's original `y = 0; if (cond) { ...; y = proj2.vy; } if (!cond) y = 0;` gives the right `sll/sra` narrowing but a second `lhu` reload and no `j`. What produces all three of retail's instructions is `if (proj2.vy < 0) { y = 0; } else { y = proj2.vy; }` - both arms assign, so cc1 has to materialise two blocks and join them. The `sll/sra` pair that follows is just the destination being `s16` at its single use, so do not chase that separately. Measured only under gcc-2.6.0-psx (the preset that function's tail needs); the 2.7.2-cdk spellings of the same three shapes were not re-measured.

- id: d-crossjump-tail-merge-stops | keywords: move, extra, cross-jump, tail-merge, duplicate, argument, block, order
  **A TAIL DUPLICATED IN BOTH ARMS OF AN IF/ELSE-IF IS WHAT PRODUCES RETAIL'S OUT-OF-LINE SHARED BLOCK, BUT gcc's CROSS-JUMP ONLY MERGES THE SUFFIX THE SCHEDULER PLACED AT THE SAME DISTANCE FROM THE END IN BOTH COPIES, so a call's argument setup that sched2 hoisted in one arm stays behind as a second copy** (round 397, battling/main/misc8 func_80080C48, parked 95.60% at 204 bytes against retail's 200 - 2 instructions, so it cannot link). Retail's block order is `[test1][arm A][j tail][test2][arm B][tail][else]` with the tail reached by fall-through from B and by a `j` (plain `nop` slot) from A; writing the 8-statement tail ONCE after the chain puts the else before it instead (79.80%, that is RTL emission order, no block reordering in 2.7.2). Duplicating the tail in both arms reproduces retail's block order exactly - the merged block sits where the second copy was - and the only residual is its first instruction: retail's merged block begins with `addu a0,zero,zero` (the BattleSetScreenMode argument, reorg then drops it into the jal slot) and arm A only has `j`+`nop`, while ours carries `move a0,zero` in A's j slot and hoisted the same move above the store in B, so cross-jump merges from the jal onward and the setup is emitted twice. Measured dead ends on that exact body: tail written once with the else returning first 79.80, a local variable for the argument, else with and without `return`, a 3-minute permuter run (base=221 best=221 - the merge boundary is not reachable by source edits), -O3 0.0, 2.7.2-cdk 85.36.
- id: d-crossjump-survivor-is-the-last | keywords: cross-jump, tail, duplicate, block, order, merge, arm, park
  **gcc 2.6.0's CROSS-JUMP MERGE TARGET IS THE FUNCTION'S LAST BLOCK WITH THAT SUFFIX, so a tail written in every arm merges into the arm the front end emitted last; retail that instead shows a STANDALONE shared tail (two arms merged into a block placed where the second arm's copy was, a third arm keeping its own) was not reachable from six source shapes** (round 403, lib_gear/main/main func_801E5CD8, parked 71.67% at 88 bytes against retail's 108, 22 insns of 27). Retail is `[test0 bnez][arm 0][test1 beq arm1][test2 bne exit][arm 2][shared tail: lhu/sll, reached by arm 0's j and arm 2's fall-through][arm 1, its own full lw/lw/lhu/sll copy][jr ra]`, with a `nop` in the `bnez` delay slot; every spelling merged arms 0 and 2 into arm 1's copy instead (5 insns short) and put the next test's `li v0,1` in the `bnez` slot. Measured, all 88 bytes unless noted: three separate `return`s in an 0,1,2 `if` chain 61.93; one local + one return in 0,2,1 order 64.59; `if (type != 0) {nested} else {...}` 0.00 at 96 (arm 0 ends up last behind a `beqz`); the nested 0,1,2 form 54.41 at 96; `if (t == 0) A; else if (t != 1) {...} else B` 71.67 - the only shape that reproduces retail's test ORDER and POLARITY (`beq` for type==1, `bne` for type==2, arm 1 emitted last), and what the residual is measured on; duplicating the tail into each arm as well 71.67. A `switch` on the parameter was 19.74. Identical under gcc-2.7.2-cdk, so the split is not a preset. Round 415 re-measured the other direction with `cc1_probe.sh` - trying to make arm 1's tail rtx-DIFFERENT so jump.c would leave it alone: `& 0xFFFF` on the loaded field, `* 0x10000`, `(x << 8) << 8`, a `u32 id = pTexture->unk14; return id << 16;` local, a `u16* pId = &pTexture->unk14; return *pId << 16;` alias, and a `goto` label placed INSIDE arm 2's then-body that arm 0 jumps to (with and without a separate `goto arm1`). All seven compile to the same 20 insns / one merged tail with the survivor at arm 1's copy: cse2 and combine fold every spelling before the cross-jump runs, so the second copy cannot be spelled from source and this stub is BLOCKED at 71.67 - do not reopen it without a new compiler.
- id: d-a-shared-tail-store-needs-both-threads-identical | keywords: cross-jump, tail, store, delay-slot, duplicate, j, fall-through, arms
  **jump.c WILL NOT CROSS-JUMP A STORE FROM ONE ARM ONTO THE OTHER ARM'S COPY WHEN THE TWO THREADS END DIFFERENTLY (one arm jumps to the epilogue, the other falls into it), AND ADDING `return;` TO BOTH ARMS TO MAKE THEM SYMMETRIC IS CANONICALISED AWAY BEFORE jump.c RUNS** (round 450, field/main/misc10 func_8009635C, parked 96.70% at retail's exact 176 bytes / 44 insns, Default preset, every register retail's: nItemId `$s1`, nIndex `$s0`, pIds `$s2`, the `-1` `$s3`, pQuantities `$s4`, byte `$a0`, address `$v1`, value `$v0`). Retail ends `beqz` (>= 99 -> epilogue, `nop` in the slot) / `j` to the `sb` that closes the else arm (`addiu v0,a0,1` in the slot): ONE store, reached by the jump and by the else arm's fall-through. Every body emits the store in the `j` delay slot and jumps past the shared block instead (three `sb` against retail's two, paid for by retail's nop), i.e. reorg copies it because the target block has no second label use. Measured on the same body: two `pQuantities[nIndex]` stores (96.70), both arms storing `pQuantity[0]` (96.59, pIds/pQuantities mirror), both arms storing a shared `nQuantity` (86.70 - value and address roles flip: address `$a0`, value `$v1`), the same plus `return;` at the end of both arms (byte-identical to 86.70), the pointer assigned before vs after the ids store (byte-identical), one store after the join (90.41, 92.16 with a pointer local). Presets: 2.6.0 96.93, cdk 96.70, -O3 does not compile. Companion, useful on its own: **a `u_char *p = &p[i]` POINTER LOCAL is what fixes a mirrored callee-saved pair here** - the subscript form puts nItemId in `$s0`/nIndex in `$s1`, the pointer form gives retail's `$s1`/`$s0` - so try the address-into-a-pointer spelling before the allocator levers on a two-arm byte writer.
- id: d-combine-folds-mask-eq-zero | keywords: move, copy, andi, sltiu, combine, bit-test, block, delay, mask
  **gcc 2.6.0's COMBINE FOLDS `(x & K) == 0` INTO `srl/xori/andi` ONLY WHEN THE ANDI AND THE COMPARE SHARE A BASIC BLOCK - put the mask in the PRE-BRANCH block and retail's `andi/sltiu/move` shape comes back** (round 397, menu/main/misc9 func_801DCE60, parked 97.64%: the plain `if (flags & 0x4000) row = 2; else row = (flags & 0x1000) == 0;` folds the else to `srl v1,12; xori; andi` where retail has `andi v0,v1,0x1000` IN THE beqz DELAY SLOT, then `sltiu v0,v0,1; move v1,v0`). Two spellings defeat the fold: `masked = flags & 0x1000;` as a statement BEFORE the `if` (blocks the combine, 96.69% - but the andi then precedes the condition's andi, so reorg fills the delay slot with the else's one remaining sltiu and the branch INVERTS, 480 insns), and the one that reproduces retail's whole shape at 97.28%: `cond = flags & 0x4000; masked = flags & 0x1000;` (both u_int statements, in that order) then `if (cond) row = 2; else row = masked == 0;` - the mask is the LAST insn before the branch, so reorg hoists the `andi 0x1000` into the beqz delay slot exactly like retail, and the sltiu stays in the else block. Residual there: ours loads flags into $v0 and cond into $v1 (retail: flags $v1, cond+masked $v0) and lacks the `move v1,v0` (a `tmp = masked == 0; row = tmp;` split is UNTRIED). u16 cond/masked reorder the andis and invert the branch again (96.69%). Preset ruled out: 2.7.2 96.93, cdk compile-fails, -G0/-O3 no change.
  Round 424 closed the fold with a spelling that also KEEPS every register retail has, found by permuter (base 1605 -> 1112) and confirmed by hand: compare against the destination variable itself - `row = 1; row = (flags & 0x1000) < row;` (same value, `row = (flags & 0x1000) == 0`). The compare's operand is now a variable, so combine cannot turn the AND into a shift; retail's `andi 0x1000` lands IN the beqz delay slot and the else keeps a lone `sltiu v0,v0,1` - 97.81% at 1924 bytes / 481 insns against retail's 1928 / 482, i.e. everything is retail's except the `move v1,v0` (retail keeps the compare result in `$v0` and copies it into row's `$v1`; ours coalesces them, and the same body then wants `$s1/$s2/$s0` where retail has `$s0/$s1/$s2` in the two call setups). Measured on the same block and dead: `< 1` for `== 0` (folds identically, 97.66 at retail's exact 482/1928), `!(...)`, a u_char/u_int boolean temp assigned then copied to row (the truncation of a proven-0/1 value is deleted, 97.81/97.52), and `row` declared u8 (483 insns). So on this shape: block the fold with a self-referential compare, then count the missing instruction - if it is the boolean copy, the park is one allocator tie from a match.

- id: d-nested-else-keeps-a-case-copy | keywords: duplicate, store, cross-jump, merge, nested, else, arm, polarity
  **ONE DUPLICATED `x = 0` IN A SWITCH ARM SURVIVES retail's CROSS-JUMP ONLY WHEN THE ARM IS SPELLED AS `if (A) { if (B) S; } else { S; }` - the test's POLARITY, not the statement set, is the load-bearing part** (round 404, menu/main/misc9 func_801DC3D8, 99.81 -> 100.00 at retail's exact 2696 bytes / 674 insns, gcc-2.6.0/XenoMenu260). The residue was 3 instructions in one spot: retail's case-0 shade test is `bnez v0,<shared test> / nop / j <join> / move s2,zero` (its OWN copy of the store, in the `j` delay slot) and every other spelling gives `beqz v0,<shared store> / nop / j <shared test> / nop` - same insn count, one store block merged instead of kept. Measured on the same body, all 99.81 / 674 insns: `if (mask == 0 || available < value) S;`, `if (mask == 0) { S; break; } if (available < value) S;` (two statements, so cross-jump merges both copies), `if (mask == 0) { S; } else if (available < value) { S; }`, AND the mirror `if (mask == 0) { S; } else { if (available < value) { S; } }`. What matched is the negated test with the nested second test in its TRUE arm and the plain store as the else: `if (mask != 0) { if (available < value) { shade = 0; } } else { shade = 0; }` - the same shape retail's `bnez` shows. So when a diff's only residue is "retail keeps a second copy of one store, ours merged it", write the arm so the branch retail's `bnez`/`beqz` implies is the one holding the nested test; equal-statement spellings are not equal.
- id: a-duplicated-guard-load-and-a | keywords: cross-jump, duplicate, copy, if-else, ternary, jal, arm, g_menu
  **A TERNARY FED TO A CALL AS "TWO FULL IDENTICAL CALLS IN AN if/else" IS CROSS-JUMPED BY gcc 2.6.0 INTO ONE jal, AND THAT IS what PUTS THE GUARD'S GLOBAL LOAD IN BOTH ARMS** (round 400, menu/main/misc4_260 func_801CF8D8, 79.94 -> 91.32 at 612 of retail's 620 bytes). Retail shows ONE `jal`, yet loads `lui/lw g_Menu` + `lw a2,0x32C(v0)` in BOTH arms of the index test, stores one stack arg in both arms, sets `li a1,0x115` in the `beqz` delay slot and merges the shared tail at the arm's own `j`. Every single-call spelling (`cond ? A : B` as one argument) hoists that load and the whole prologue into one block, 4-5 instructions short - no amount of type or order tweaking recovers it. Writing the if/else around two complete, identical calls (only the differing argument changes) reproduces it in one edit. Try this on any diff where retail has one `jal` for two near-identical call sites; the residual after it is then a pure allocator tie.
- id: d-entry-param-copy-survives | keywords: move, copy, parameter, entry, prologue, copy-propagation, reassign
  **A `move <arg-reg>,<param-reg>` AT ENTRY (retail copies a parameter out and then reuses the parameter's own register for a value derived from it, `sll a1,a1,16 / sra / addiu` IN PLACE) IS PRODUCED BY A LOCAL INITIALISED FROM THE PARAMETER THAT IS REASSIGNED IN A LATER ARM - and only then, because the reassignment is what stops copy-propagation coalescing the two.** (round 400, slus_006.64/system/animation_scripts func_800223B0, parked 74.09% at 668 bytes / 167 insns against retail's 688 / 172). Writing `s32 index = animId;` at the top and letting cases 1/2 overwrite `index` with the group index gives the entry copy (`move a0,a1`, 74.09%); a named parity local `s32 parity = ((s16)animId + 0x400) & 1` is worth the same +0.55 over the inline test (73.54 -> 74.09, same 668 bytes); but writing the case-1/2 casts off `index` instead of `animId` makes the copy's initial value dead on those paths, gcc coalesces it away and the copy disappears entirely (73.54%, 664 bytes). Which register the copy takes is then not source-controllable on that body: declaration order moved it between `$a0` and `$a2` (both 74.09) and never to retail's `$a3`, so the arms' temporaries all shift and the park is 5 `move`s short - park that residual, do not permute it.

- id: d-sign-known-ternary-folds | keywords: abs, negu, move, ternary, bgez, fold, builtin, instruction
  **AN ABS WRITTEN AS A TERNARY INSIDE AN ARM THAT ALREADY ESTABLISHED THE SIGN FOLDS TO A BARE `negu` AND DROPS RETAIL'S `addu v1,s1,zero`, LEAVING THE BODY ONE INSTRUCTION SHORT; `__builtin_abs(x)` KEEPS THE PAIR** (round 404, battling/main/misc3 func_80076884, 97.34 -> 99.43 at retail's exact 1972 bytes / 491 instructions). Retail's analog-stick clamp is the two-step `bgez s1,L` / `addu v1,s1,zero` (delay slot) / `negu v1,v1`; inside `if (stickY2 < 0 && enabled)` cc1 knows the sign, so `limit = stickY2 < 0 ? -stickY2 : stickY2;` compiles to the single `negu v1,s1` and the function cannot link even at 99.x. The extra `negative = stickY2 < 0;` local *does* defeat the range knowledge (bgez/addu/negu) but raises the register pressure enough to flip two other variables (+8 bytes), and applying it to both clamp arms lands 8 bytes over. `limit = __builtin_abs(stickY2);` is the same source-level intent, compiles the two-step form, and costs nothing elsewhere - so any abs whose retail body has a `bgez` plus a move-plus-negu pair wants the builtin, not the ternary.

- id: d-an-abs-needs-one-local-per-site | keywords: abs, builtin, negu, move, bgez, local, per-site, coalesce
  **A REPEATED `__builtin_abs` PATTERN NEEDS ONE ABS LOCAL PER SITE, NOT ONE SHARED ONE: the wins are nested** (round 405, world_map/main/misc12 func_80090FB4, 98.03 -> 98.52 with `__builtin_abs` and 98.52 -> 98.71 with a second local, retail's exact 1148 bytes / 287 insns all the way). Retail shows `bgez v0,L / move a1,v0 / negu a1,a1 / L: slt v0,a1,t0` in two different ramp blocks, and cc1 gives that pair ONLY for `__builtin_abs(x)` - the ternaries and if/else spellings of the same abs are coalesced to one pseudo before allocation (a `-dc` dump of the unit shows `(reg 87) = 107 - 82` then `(reg 87) = -(reg 87)`), so reorg target-fills the bgez slot with a duplicate `slt` and the body is one instruction short. With `nAbs = __builtin_abs(nDelta);` shared by both blocks the two sites get the SAME register (`v1` twice) where retail uses `a1` then `a0`; splitting it into `nAbs` and `nAbsVel` gives `a1` then `a0` and makes the second ramp block instruction-identical. Measured dead ends on the same body: `if (d >= 0) { a = d; } else { a = -d; }` DOES keep a copy (`bgez / move rd,rs / negu rd,rs`, the right shape) but costs 5 instructions elsewhere (95.78%, 292 insns), and an if/else of either polarity without the builtin, `u32 nPad`, reading the pad global inline at all five tests, and block-scoped locals all leave the residual register rotation alone.
- id: d-builtin-abs-shape-but-one-shot-short | keywords: abs, builtin, negu, move, bgez, scheduling, rotated, short
  **`__builtin_abs` DOES buy retail's `bgez rd,Rn` / `move Rw,Rn` / `negu Rw,Rw` shape, but on a scheduled cdk unit it also moves the `sll` of an unrelated constant past the `bgez` and leaves the body one instruction short - so an abs-shaped residual can be right in shape and still unlinkable** (round 419, battle/main/misc28 func_800B65B0, XenoBattleTailCdk0, re-parked once measured). Retail's two angle-clamp blocks are `sll a1,v0,0x2 / bgez v1,L / move v0,v1 / negu v0,v0 / slt v0,a1,v0`; the do-while magnitude body that matches the 600 bytes / 150 insns gives `move v0,v1 / negu v0,v1` (negate of the SOURCE), and `mag = __builtin_abs(delta);` gives retail's pair but 596 bytes / 149 insns with rate's `sll` after the branch, scored 95.50. Per-site locals (`deltaY/deltaZ/magY/magZ/turnY/turnZ`) with the builtin: 604 / 151 and both abs shapes right, but rate takes `$a0` where retail has `$a1` and sched1 inserts two load nops. Plain `turn = delta; mag = delta;` in both blocks: 604 / 151 (one pseudo for both blocks' delta gets copied twice). Read this as: the builtin is the right spelling for the SHAPE and the wrong one for the SIZE whenever the unit has `-fschedule-insns`.
- id: d-value-both-arms-compute | keywords: move, copy, join, if-else, phi, temp, shared, sized
  **A VALUE BOTH ARMS OF AN IF/ELSE COMPUTE BECOMES ONE COPY AT THE JOIN ONLY WHEN IT GOES THROUGH A WIDER TEMP: give a `u16` local an `s32` temp that both arms assign and one `local = temp;` after the if/else** (round 405, battle/main/misc25 func_800B026C, 2124 -> retail's exact 2120 bytes / 530 insns, parked 95.81%). Retail's first actor/table block computes `y` per arm (`subu` in the jal delay slot on one path, late on the other) yet copies it ONCE at the merge (`addu s2,v1,zero` at 0xF8); assigning the `u16 y` directly in both arms makes cc1 emit one copy in EACH arm, exactly 4 bytes too many. With `s32 dy;` assigned in both arms and `y = dy;` after the if/else the copies collapse to retail's single join copy and the size is exact. Both operands need their `(u16)` casts or the loads come out `lh` where retail has `lhu`. The same body's remaining residual is the callee-saved rotation, which is a wall: `-dg`/`-dl` show the block pointer at 75 refs over 276 insns against 48/160, 39/135 and 48/180 for the three `u16` components, so it takes `$s1` where retail has `$s5` and no spelling moves it.

- id: a-register-arg-constant-la-is-deferred | keywords: la, move, constant, argument, expand, call, placement, park
  **A REGISTER-PASSED CONSTANT ARGUMENT'S `la` IS EMITTED IN load_register_parameters, i.e. AFTER every memory argument's load - so retail materialising the address FIRST means that argument was not a compile-time constant, and no single-use local can say so** (round 408, movie/main/misc1 func_800704E8, parked 99.04% and then 99.28% at retail's exact 2276 bytes / 569 insns once the preheader `li` was fixed - see g-front-end-statement-places-a-preheader; residual below is the `la` row plus that trade). The function has eighteen `FontPrintf(fmt, ...)` calls whose `la a0,fmt` sits LAST among the argument loads in both retail and our build; at one site retail has it FIRST and ours last (`lui a0,%hi(D_8006FC40)/addiu` before `lw a1..a3` instead of after). Reading cc1's own output (`build/src/<unit>.c.s`) shows ours is `lw $5/$6/$7/$2` then `la $4`, and `la $4` first at the one call retail agrees with; the mechanism is that expand_expr emits nothing for a SYMBOL_REF argument, so the move surfaces late, while a non-constant argument is materialised at its own statement - which is where retail's early `la` sits. A single-use pointer local is folded back into the constant (measured twice, 99.04 and identical rows), so the only surviving route is to make the argument a real value, which changes the instruction count. Two more negatives on the same body: moving `pCounters = ...; pBase = ...;` from before the loop into the loop's first two statements is hoisted straight back to the identical preheader slot (cc1 emits `la s4 / move s2 / li s3` either way), and declaring those two locals first changes nothing - Round 423 closed it with `cc1_probe.sh` on a standalone copy of that block: six spellings of the
call (bare symbol, `char* pFormat = D_8006FC40;` as the block's first statement, a `(char*)` cast,
`&D_8006FC40[0]`, `D_8006FC40 + 0`, and the pointer assigned ABOVE the branch) emit
`lw a1 / lw a2 / lw a3 / lw v0 / la a0` under BOTH gcc-2.6.0 and gcc-2.7.2; the only spelling that
hoists the `la` is the pre-branch assignment, which puts it above the `slt`/`beqz` instead of
inside the block - so retail's in-block la-first order is unreachable from any call spelling, while
the preheader `li sN,K` at that function did move (see g-front-end-statement-places-a-preheader),
so a preheader whose hoisted `li` sits LAST where retail has it FIRST is a front-end-statement
question first, and only then the same class: park after reading the cc1 .s rather than permuting (a 4-minute run from this identical body: base 460 = best 460, because the reloc-name rows dominate its score).

- id: a-statement-between-the-test-and-the-call-reorders-the-format-argument | keywords: la, argument, call, constant, placement, statement, order, block
  **A CONSTANT-ADDRESS ARGUMENT'S `la` IS EMITTED FIRST, NOT LAST, WHEN THE CALL IS THE FIRST STATEMENT OF ITS BLOCK - so a retail `la a0,fmt` above the other argument loads means a statement that used to sit between the `if` test and the call has to move INSIDE the block after it, and the previous entry's "unreachable from any call spelling" was really a statement-position problem** (round 436, movie/main/misc1 func_800704E8, 99.28 -> 99.66 -> 100.00 with `check_bin.sh movie` OK at retail's exact 2276 bytes / 569 insns, XenoBattle260Div = gcc-2.6.0). Retail's stream-status `FontPrintf(D_8006FC40, g_MovieDebugCounters, g_MovieStreamBlock, g_MovieStreamWritePtr, g_MovieStreamBytesLeft)` emits `lui/addiu a0` before `lw a1..a3`, and every body with `i = 0;` immediately before the `if (g_MovieStreamCommand >= 0xB || ...)` puts it after them; moving that one statement to the top of the if-body (its value cannot be observed before the loop below it) puts the `la` first and is worth the row, with `move s0,zero`/`addu s0,zero,zero` no longer in the `beqz` delay slot. Six call spellings (bare symbol, pointer local, cast, `&arr[0]`, `+ 0`, pre-branch assignment) measured identical before that, so when a diff shows one `la` of a constant argument in the wrong slot, look at the STATEMENTS AROUND the call before the call's own spelling.

- id: b-assignment-used-as-the-while | keywords: move, copy, delay, while, condition, loop, mask, reload
  **A `move sN,v0` IN THE BRANCH DELAY SLOT BEFORE A LOOP BODY MEANS THE VALUE IS THE `while` CONDITION'S OWN ASSIGNMENT (`while ((x = expr) != 0)`): cc1 tests the temp and copies it into `x`'s home register in the delay slot, where a separate `x = expr;` statement assigns straight into the home register and leaves a `nop`** (round 415, slus_006.64/psyq/libetc/intr trapIntr, 96.06 -> 100.00 at retail's exact 488 bytes / 122 insns, PSY-Q preset). Retail re-reads `enabledInterruptsMask` at BOTH copies of the condition (`and v1,v1,a0` / `and v0,v0,v1` / `beqz v0` / `move s0,v0` at the entry and again at the loop bottom, rotating the loop over the body), which a hoisted `mask = enabled & ...;` before the loop cannot produce: the load has to be inside the condition so it reappears per iteration. Spelling it `mask = enabled & *g_pI_STAT; inInterrupt = 1; mask &= *g_pI_MASK; while (mask) { body; mask = ...; }` scores 96.06 with the SAME 122 insns and frame, differing only in the missing delay-slot copy and which register the final `and` lands in - so when a park's residual is exactly "one move in the delay slot plus a mirrored `and` operand", check whether the source's loop condition recomputes the value instead of a statement before the loop.

- id: d-copy-then-mask-in-place-is-a-combine-miss | keywords: move, copy, andi, mask, store, combine, park, one-short
  **A RETAIL `move v1,v0` / `sh v1,<raw global>` / `andi v1,v1,<mask>` TRIPLE THAT SITS AROUND A RAW STORE IS THE PESSIMAL FORM OF `andi v1,v0,<mask>`, AND cc1 2.7.2 ONLY EMITS IT WHEN THE STORE SEPARATES THE COPY FROM THE MASK - no source spelling reaches it, so a park one instruction short with this shape is BLOCKED** (round 420, field/main/misc func_8008EC30, re-parked 94.74% at 480 bytes against retail's 484: retail copies the halfword argument, stores it raw, then masks the COPY in place for the flags and masks the ORIGINAL in place for the type, while ours folds the copy into the flags `andi` (one instruction fewer, so the body can never link) and zero-extends the masked value for the switch). Combine folds `move`+`andi` into one instruction whenever the copy has a single use; retail's copy has two (the raw store and the mask), and the store between them is what blocks the fold - nothing in C says "make a copy the store and a later mask share", so gcc emits the shorter body every time. Measured, all on the same body: `u16 type` (assigned either side of the raw store) 480 bytes but 86.97% (gcc masks into a fresh register and stores from v0), flags store before the type mask with `D_800C3A30 = type;` 476 / 90.16, `D_800C3A30 &= 0xF` vs `= type` no help, and `-O3` via try_flags identical at 94.74. Read a "copy + in-place mask" park as BLOCKED; the size miss is not an allocator question.
- id: d-parameter-copy-and-in-place-narrow-chain | keywords: move, copy, parameter, in-place, sll, sra, sign-extend, argument
  **A RETAIL PROLOGUE THAT COPIES THE ARGUMENT AND THEN SIGN-EXTENDS THE ARGUMENT'S OWN REGISTER IN PLACE (`addu a3,a1,zero` for the long-lived use, `sll a1,a1,16 / sra / addiu K`) IS NOT REACHABLE FROM SOURCE IN cc1 2.7.2 WHATEVER VARIABLE THE CHAIN IS WRITTEN THROUGH - the chain always materialises in a fresh `$v0`** (round 426, slus_006.64/system/animation_scripts func_800223B0, parked 74.33% at 668 bytes against retail's 688 - 5 instructions, so it cannot link; XenoGraphics preset, cdk -O2). Four spellings measured on the same body, all 664 bytes / 166-167 insns: `parity = (s16)animId + 0x400; if (parity & 1)` with the argument copy declared first (73.77), `parity = animId; parity = (s16)parity + 0x400;` so the chain destroys its input and the copy should become necessary (73.77 - copy-prop folds the two initialisations into one expression and the `move a3,a1` still never appears), the chain split into `parity = (s16)animId; parity += 0x400;`, and assigning the result back INTO the parameter (`animId = parity; if (animId & 1)`) - which is the one form that should make the parameter's register self-modifying, and does not (73.77). The parked body also records the m2c draft shape at 60.69 / 596 / 149 insns (flag value through a temp variable, both arm tests inverted, body wrapped in `if (p != NULL)`) losing the tail-merged case-arm block, and the round-413 set (declaration order moves the copy between `$a0`/`$a2`, never `$a3`; 2.6.0 71.56; cdk -O3 rejected by cc1). So when a diff's whole residual is "retail's argument register is reused in place for a narrow-and-add chain, ours gets a fresh `$v0` and the arms lose their two `move`s", it is a front-end/allocator artifact: park it after one head-of-function look, do not permute (74% with 20 bytes missing, and the missing instructions are not source statements).

- id: d-derived-biv-for-a-call-argument-survives-every-loop-spelling | keywords: move, copy, biv, latch, preheader, loop, argument, park
  **A LOOP WHOSE CALL ARGUMENT IS A `move`/`addu` COPY OF THE WALKING POINTER GETS TWO COPIES FROM loop.c (one in the preheader, one in the latch) WHATEVER THE SOURCE SPELLING IS - the preheader one is the derived biv's init and the latch one is its increment, so a park one instruction long on exactly that copy is loop.c's decision, not the construct's** (round 441, field/main/misc2 func_80074108, re-parked 97.44% at 1432 bytes / 358 insns against retail's 1428 / 357; XenoFieldNoGp = gcc-2.7.2 -O2 -G0). cc1's own output shows the pair (`move $5,$17` above the loop label and in the `bne` slot), where retail's loop has ONE copy inside the body and the block after the loop's `lui` in the slot. Measured on the same body, all 358 insns: the indexed subscript `&D_800B0FEC[i - 0x15]` (97.44, the best), a `do { call(pQuad); pQuad++; i++; } while (i < 0x19)` walking pointer (96.31, and it swaps the counter/pointer `$s0`/`$s1` roles), a `pQuad = D_800B0FEC;` preheader plus `pQuad++` as the body's last statement (96.34), and a fresh `pCurrentQuad = &D_800B0FEC[i - 0x15];` as the body's FIRST statement (byte-identical to the indexed form - copy propagation eats it, the same way it eats an alias pointer). Not a preset fact: gcc-2.7.2-cdk 78.20, gcc-2.6.0 97.44 with the identical shape, 2.7.2 -O3 compile-fails. The tell that separates this from `g-biv-init-and-latch-move-together` (where the derived giv is retail's) is that the SAME function's neighbouring loop keeps init+latch in retail too: when two loops in one body disagree, the difference is a property of one subscript expression (here the `- 0x15` additive offset), so park and say which expression, rather than re-spelling the loop.

- id: d-a-two-step-base-chain-folds | keywords: move, copy, chain, intermediate, copy-propagation, base, three, parameter
  **A RETAIL THREE-COPY CHAIN OF ONE BASE POINTER (`move t1,a0 / move s0,t1 / ... / move s1,s0`) CANNOT BE SPELLED FROM C: gcc 2.7.2's copy propagation deletes the intermediate variable, and forcing the third copy with a local assigned after a call rotates the whole callee-saved set instead** (round 442, battling/main/misc14 func_8008B13C, parked 97.42% at 588 bytes against retail's 592 - the one missing instruction IS the copy, so the body can never link; Default preset, structure settled, both jump tables in place). Retail keeps the script/cfg base in `$t1` (one use: feeding `$s0`), then in `$s0` (live across the two heap calls) and makes a THIRD copy `move s1,s0` after the `HeapAlloc`, which the two `pScript + value` sums use; the parked body has two (`move s1,a0 / move s0,s1` at entry). Measured on that body: `u8 *pBase = pScript; Misc14AnimCfg *pCfg = (Misc14AnimCfg *)pBase;` with `pBase = (u8 *)pCfg;` after the HeapAlloc and the sums written off `pBase` gives `move s0,a0` ONLY (no entry temp, no `move s1,s0`) because pBase's first value has a single use, and because pScript's pseudo then dies at entry the callee-saved set rotates (pAnim `$s3`->`$s2`, pNodes `$s2`->`$s1`, frame 0x38->0x30): 92.54% / 580 bytes. So the two-pseudo form is unreachable while both pseudos must survive copy propagation; park on the byte count instead of hunting the copy. Related, measured before on the same body: a base local initialised at entry coalesces (95.37), the parameter typed as the cfg with `u8 *pScript = (u8 *)pCfg;` coalesces (95.11), and the permuter's randomizer refuses this body.

- id: a-second-live-pointer-variable-forces-retail-copy | keywords: move, copy, missing, second, pointer, coalesce, live-range, split
  **A MISSING `move sN,sM` COPY OF A LOOP POINTER IS FORCED BY GIVING THE LOOP ITS OWN POINTER VARIABLE ASSIGNED FROM THE FIRST ONE; a single variable is always coalesced away, and gcc only emits the copy when the second pointer's live range crosses a call** (round 451, lib_gear/main/main func_801E5D44, 95.92 -> 97.61% and 1508 -> 1512 bytes = retail's exact size / 378 insns, XenoLibraryDiv). Retail's case 8 is `addu s6,s0,zero` (copy pCmd) then `addu s0,zero,zero` (the loop counter takes pCmd's register) and the loop reads `lbu a3,0x5(s6)`; ours kept pCmd in s0 and gave the counter a free s1, so the copy and 4 bytes were absent. Function-scope `ModelScriptCommand* pCommand;` + `pCommand = pCmd;` at the arm entry makes gcc keep both and emit the copy. The same trick in an arm with NO call (case 2 of the same function) is coalesced and byte-identical, so use it where the second pointer crosses a call.

## sign/zero extension and narrow types

symptoms: andi 0xff or sll-sra present/absent, lbu vs lb, width
try: andi lbu lhu narrow extension
- id: e-a-sign-extend-pair-implied-by-casts | keywords: s16, sign_extend, sltu, slti, andi, cast, range, index
  **gcc 2.6.0 FOLDS A `(s16)` CAST AWAY WHEN IT KNOWS THE VALUE CAME FROM A `lbu`, SO RETAIL'S `sll 16`/`sra 16` PAIR PROVES THE DISPATCH VALUE'S RANGE WAS *UNKNOWN* - and the same narrow type cannot be the one that feeds a table index, because the cast forms differ** (round 442, slus_006.64/system/sound func_8003C6E8, XenoLibrary = gcc-2.6.0, parked 75.84%). `cc1_probe.sh` over four spellings of one dispatch (`if (cmd < 0x80) return; if (cmd == 0x90) ...; p += table[(s16)(cmd - 0x80)]`): `u8 cmd` gives `andi 0xff` + **`sltu`** and retail's 3-instruction index (`addu r,cmd,-128 / sll 16 / sra 14`); `s16 cmd` gives a signed compare plus the HImode value but collapses the index to `addu / sll 2`; `int cmd` and `int cmd` with `(s16)cmd` in the chain both **drop the cast entirely** and compare the bare byte. So: a `sltu` on a byte test means the source declared the value unsigned (retail's bare signed `slti` means `s32`/`int`), and when retail materialises a sign-extension of a byte the sign-extended value is NOT that byte - it came from memory as `s16` or from a variable with other, wider assignments. Check the cast before blaming the allocator: if gcc folded your cast, no spelling of that cast will reproduce retail's pair.
- id: e-the-incoming-copy-width-names-the-parameter-type | keywords: parameter, argument, incoming, copy, lw, lhu, s16, s32, word
  **A STACK PARAMETER'S INCOMING COPY NAMES ITS DECLARED WIDTH: `lw off(sp)` into a frame slot (or a callee-saved register) with a later `sll 16`/`sra 16` at the use is a 32-bit parameter, while `lhu off(sp)` + `sh slot` is an `s16` - so a head that disagrees with retail on exactly those copies is a signature question, not an allocator one** (round 433, lib_gear/main/main func_801E1A14, parked 72.58 -> 76.11%: 4 of the 20 arguments moved from `s16` to `s32` - `rowBase`, `scale`, `texX`-style offsets and the section count - and the frame came down from 0x138 to retail's exact 0x130). Retail copies the two word values straight into `$s6`/`$s5` with no `sll`/`sra` anywhere (`mult v0,s5`), and it narrows the section count only at its two uses, the `sh` store into the s16 field and the `if (field > 0)` test that follows it - which is why the source reads the stored field back (`pEntry->numSections`) instead of the parameter: gcc narrows the just-stored value with `lw`/`sll 16`/`sra 16` from the parameter's home rather than `lh`-ing the field. Same file, same function: `-O1` and `-O3` do not build under this unit's preset, so a 6-point jump from two signature edits is worth a `try_flags.sh` look before any spelling hunt.
- id: e-one-word-read-as-lbu-and-lhu-proves-a-byte-pointer | keywords: lbu, lhu, byte, pointer, field, row, typing, cast
  **WHEN RETAIL READS THE SAME ADDRESS AS `lbu` IN ONE ARM AND `lhu` IN ANOTHER, THE SOURCE WENT THROUGH A BYTE POINTER, NOT A TYPED FIELD - so a "type the row" rewrite that the neighbouring functions use is unavailable at that site, whatever the struct says the field is** (round 452, battle/main/misc30 func_800B9284, re-parked 96.61% / 644 bytes = retail's exact size). The command rows are `BattleCommandRow` (size 0x48) and `BATTLE_COMMAND_ROW(i)` is the unit's own idiom, but the dispatch reads its `+0x172` word as `lbu a0,370(v0)` in the 0xFA/0xF8 arms (the value handed to BattleSetSlotFlagB4/BattleClearSlotFlagB4) and as `lhu` in the 0xF7/0xF5/0xF4/0xF3 arms - a u16 field access would make every one of those a `lhu`, so the parked body's `u8 *pSlot = g_BattleStateWork + g_BattleCommandRowIndex * 0x48;` plus per-arm `*(u16 *)(pSlot + 0x172)` / `pSlot[0x172]` is the honest spelling. Also measured on that body: a `u16 id` local for the halfword read is 95.99 against the `s32 id` body's 96.61, so the width of a local that only feeds `>> 9` / `& 0x1FF` stays s32.
- id: e-a-byte-derived-volume-declared | keywords: short, s16, narrow, copy, move, callee-saved, volume
  **A VOLUME THAT RETAIL COPIES BEFORE ITS ZERO TEST (`move v0,s2 / beqz v0 / mult v0,v0`) AND THEN ADDS IN PLACE IS A `short`, NOT AN `s32` - the narrow declaration is what gives the copy, the callee-saved assignment and retail's exact size in one edit** (round 411, slus_006.64/system/sound func_8003E04C, 84.75 -> 90.34 at retail's exact 244 bytes / 61 insns under the unit's gcc-2.6.0; a permuter run found the type, and the same body with `s32 volume` had been parked since round 3xx). The two bodies differ only in that declaration and the s32 one loads `lbu a1,0(s4)` where retail has `lbu s2,0(s4)` plus the copy. Companion find from the same round, same function: an `AudioEnvelope*` local used for every store AND the `SoundResetEnvelope` call (rather than subscripting `pAudioElements->envelopes[2]` each time) is worth another 2 points (90.34 -> 92.30) because it holds the envelope base in the callee-saved register retail uses and lets the `addiu a0,s1,0x280` land where retail has it. Residual after both: retail keeps the mode byte in `$s3` and forms the index in the call's `$a2` then copies it back (`andi a2,s3,0xf / move s3,a2`), so its `jal` slot holds `sra a1,a1,0x10` while ours holds `move a2,s3` - a mode/index register-sharing decision, not a spelling tried (index hoisted before the volume test: 89.02 with 14 differing lines; flags before index: 87.34; `mode & 0xF` inline in all three uses: 80.20; index assigned after the call: 90.54).
- id: e-a-missing-0xffff-mask-on | keywords: andi, mask, u16, prototype, declaration, call, k&r, implicit
  **A CALL ARGUMENT RETAIL PASSES UNMASKED WHERE OURS GETS `andi a2,<reg>,0xffff` IS PROTOTYPE VISIBILITY, NOT A C SHAPE: an `int` expression passed to a `u_short` parameter is truncated by the caller whenever the callee's ANSI declaration is in scope, and passed raw when the call site sees no prototype.** Measured round 404, shop_menu/main/misc func_801CDD14 (parked 97.85%, 478 insns against retail's 475, the 3 surplus exactly two masks plus one `addiu`): its two `ShopMenuSetVertices(..., 0x32 + FONT_LETTER_HEIGHT * i, ...)` calls mask, and the matched twin gear_shop_menu/main/render.c GearShopMenuRenderSellItemRows makes the identical calls to the same-signature GearShopMenuSetVertices (defined in ANOTHER TU) with no `extern` declaration anywhere, so gcc 2.6 treats it as an implicit declaration, applies default promotions and emits no mask - which is exactly retail's shape (`lw a2,0x80(sp)`, no `andi`). An explicit `(u_short)` cast on the argument changes NOTHING (re-measured). The mask is genuinely from the u_short parameter and not from the callee doing it: the same file's matched ShopMenuConfirmationWindowInitialize does mask its y argument, as `andi a2,a2,0xfff0` (gcc folds the 0xffff into the constant because the value has bits 0-3 clear). So inside one TU the masking follows the declaration, and a parked body whose only surplus instructions are these masks is BLOCKED unless the callee's definition can be moved after the call - check whether the twin's callee is in another TU before spending a lane on casts, `s32` locals or permuter runs (a 3-minute run and two earlier 4-minute runs found nothing here). **CORRECTION, measured on that same body later: the old-style declaration DOES work inside this TU.** `extern void ShopMenuSetVertices();` at file scope immediately above the function (exactly what the matched twin in the same file does above its own calls) plus one shared y spelling `0x32 + FONT_LETTER_HEIGHT * i` for both calls removes both masks AND the `addiu a2,<13i>,0x32`: 478 -> 476 insns, 1912 -> 1904 bytes, 97.85 -> 97.80 percent (the percentage dips only because the register cascade that follows touches half the rows). A file-scope old-style declaration mid-file is safe here - it was re-checked against every matched caller after it in the unit (two stayed at objdiff 100.00) plus `check_bin.sh shop_menu` OK - so try the declaration before calling a mask residual BLOCKED. What is left afterwards is one instruction: retail's cse constant 0x28 holds `s2` per iteration (sharing it with 0x1000, whose live range begins later) because retail has one callee-saved register spare, while a real `u32 polyColor` local takes the 9th register here, so global-alloc rematerialises the 0x28 at both uses.
- id: e-decrement-whose-asm-shows | keywords: extension, narrow, addiu
  **A decrement whose asm shows the addiu 0xFF form is not a decrement.** arr[i]-- and arr[i] -= 1 compile to the immediate -1 (0xFFFF); retail's source said arr[i] = arr[i] + 0xFF (or += 0xFF). Write the add. The same spelling already exists in matched code (battle/misc18 BattleDecrementConditionTimers), so it is house style, not a hack.
- id: e-narrow-u8-u16-parameter | keywords: extension, narrow, u8, cc1, s32, andi, cast, u16
  **A narrow `u8` (or `u16`) PARAMETER is real source, not a trick.** round 312 `battle/misc13` func_80086C88 went 76.36 -> 87.15 purely from `u8 arg0` and `u8 arg3`, because a narrow parameter makes cc1 mask the argument ONCE in place and reuse the masked value (`andi s0,s0,0xFF`), where an `s32` parameter masks twice or not at all. The disassembly of a masked argument looks exactly like an s32 that happens to be masked, so try the narrow type before concluding the register file is wrong - and spell the cast at the SECOND use too, or cc1 masks twice and the registers swap.
- id: e-u16-locals-s32-small | keywords: extension, narrow, u16, s32, sra, cc1, sll
  **`u16` locals, not `s32`, for a small amount**: round 313 `battle/misc18` func_80097610 was 96.26 with a `u32`/`s32` result and 100.00 the moment the three amounts and the running result were `u16`, because a `(u16)result * (4 - count) / 4` on a wide type makes cc1 emit `sra` where retail shifts the narrow value. When a park's residual includes a `sra`/`sll` pair around a division, change the local's width before anything else.
- id: e-do-chase-andi-reg | keywords: extension, narrow, u16, andi, cast, cc1, s32, struct
  **Do not chase an `andi reg,reg,0xFFFF` (round 323, 48 probes).** It is cc1's `zero_extendhisi2` of an HImode pseudo whose `nonzero_bits` it cannot bound - a VALUE-RANGE decision, not a habit. Every bounded spelling emits nothing (u16 local, `s32` + cast at the use, u16 parameter, u16 struct member, volatile, bitfield, `& 0xFFFF`, `index = index;`, an if/else join, a three-statement HImode form), and only an unbounded value such as `+ (u16)call()` emits it. So if your body's index is provably bounded and retail still masks, the C you can write cannot reach it - park it with the number.
- id: e-andi-ffff-kept-by | keywords: extension, narrow, u16, andi, or, register, operand, cc1
  **The `andi reg,reg,0xFFFF` of a `u16` value SURVIVES when the value's first wider use mixes it with an SImode REGISTER** (round 393, battle/main/misc17 func_80091B38 99.22 -> 100.00 and func_8009382C 99.04 -> 100.00, both at retail's exact byte count, in one edit each). Both bodies are `u16 index;` printed twice, `GetStringEntry(bank, index)` and `GetStringEntry(bank, index | 1)`; retail has ONE `andi s0,s0,0xFFFF` between the previous `jal` and the first argument load, and every bounded spelling drops it (round 323's 35, this round's base body). Declaring the OR's right operand `s32` (`s32 altEntry; altEntry = 1; ... index | altEntry`) keeps the mask: the constant 1 folds into the HImode use with cse's nonzero_bits knowledge, a register operand does not, so the `zero_extendhisi2` becomes real and is hoisted above the calls to serve BOTH uses while the machine `ori a1,s0,0x1` still emits (cse knows the register holds 1). `permute.sh` found it in 186 iterations on the first function, base 100 best 0; the identical edit closed the second. So when a park's ONLY residual is one `andi 0xFFFF` on a narrow local whose value is used in an OR/add against something the source treats as a constant, put that constant in an `s32` local: the extra local costs nothing and it is semantically inert. It is a register/operand-mode hint, so comment it and report the function ROUGH.
- id: e-park-retail-s-exact | keywords: extension, narrow, s32, u16, lbu, lhu, permuter, pointer
  A PARK AT RETAIL'S EXACT SIZE WITH ONE SWAPPED LOAD/STORE PAIR IS OFTEN A **TYPE WIDTH**, not an allocator tie (round 194). battle/misc32 func_800BD1FC sat at 96.55% through four hand rounds and a permuter run that tried statement order, comma-folding, pointer aliasing and store order - none of which could work, because retail's two loads have DIFFERENT widths (lhu 0x15C then lbu 0x174) and the C said u16 for both. Declaring the timer s32 (matching D_800C3D38) flipped the order and the function matched. Before concluding a tie is unreachable, give each local in the swapped pair the other width (u8/u16/s16/s32) and re-measure.
- id: e-narrow-parameter-flips-the | keywords: extension, narrow, u8, s32, prologue, andi, move, regalloc, param
  **A NARROW PARAMETER CAN FLIP THE PROLOGUE'S COPY-vs-MASK ORDER, not just the `andi` count** (round 389, `lib_gear/main/main` func_801E6974, 99.44 -> 100.00 in one edit, 264 instructions, byte-identical to its twin `BattleModelQueueTransformCommand`). With `s32 flags` the compile emits `move $s7,$a3` (the callee-saved copy of a parameter that is live across calls) BEFORE `andi $s1,$a3,0x7`, and rematerialises the flag's zero-extension into `$v1` at the last use; retail has `andi $s1,$a3,0x7` first, `move $s7,$a3` after, and that one zero-extend in `$a3`. Declaring the parameter `u8 flags` - the twin's own prototype - reproduces retail exactly with no other change. The tell is that retail's mask is taken from the RAW argument register before the copy is made: that is a QImode op on the parameter itself, which cc1 only emits when the parameter's declared type is narrow. So when a 99%+ body's only residual is a prologue save/copy that moved by one slot plus one scratch register on a masked value, try the narrow parameter type before any declaration reordering. Spell `flags & 0xFF` at the recursive call too (the twin does); a u8 parameter does not make that andi disappear.
- id: e-signed-vs-unsigned-sll | keywords: extension, narrow, andi, signed, sll, sra, unsigned
  Signed vs unsigned: `sll/sra` pairs vs `andi 0xff` mean a parameter or field has the wrong type.
- id: e-lhu-lbu-original-vs | keywords: extension, narrow, lbu, lhu, lw
  `lhu`/`lbu` in the original vs `lw` in yours tells you the true field width.
- id: e-andi-0xff-call-site | keywords: extension, narrow, s32, andi, permuter, u8
  AN 'andi 0xff' AT A CALL SITE DOES NOT PROVE THE CALLER'S PARAMETER IS WIDE. gcc 2.7.2 masks a NARROW parameter passed to a NARROW parameter, so if retail ANDs every call's argument, declare the parameter u8/char and the masks appear by themselves; declaring it s32 (which is what the disassembly looks like) loses them (round 228, battle/misc10 func_8007EF6C: s32 -> char took it 98.44% -> 100.00%, a one-line diff found by the permuter).

- id: e-matched-param-width-is-set-by-callers | keywords: extension, narrow, parameter, signature, caller, matched, s8, sll-sra, li
  **A SIGNATURE THAT ALREADY MEASURES 100.00% IS NOT PROOF OF THE PARAMETER WIDTH: the CALLERS decide it, and a too-narrow parameter costs every call site a mask** (round 400, field/main/misc9; approved by the orchestrator because the fix is contained in one TU). `FieldDrawScreenOverlayPrims` was declared `(s8 color)`; its body only writes the low byte, so it measured 100.00% / 976 bytes under s8. But retail's own call sites in func_800ABA98 pass `index << 3` (up to 0x80) and the literal `0x80` UNTRUNCATED (`sll a0,s0,0x3`, `li a0,0x80`) - with s8 cc1 must emit `sll a0,s0,0x1b / sra a0,a0,0x18` and `li a0,-128` at both sites. Declaring the parameter `int` restored both call sites (2 instructions, +2.37 points on the caller) and the callee re-measured 100.00% / 976 bytes unchanged. So when a caller's diff shows a `sll`/`sra` pair or a negative `li` feeding a `jal`, check the CALLEE's declared width before blaming the caller's C; a callee that reads only part of an argument matches under any width and hides the error.
- id: e-andi-0xff-after-a-jal | keywords: extension, narrow, andi, u8, return, prototype, s32, jal
  AN `andi v0,v0,0xff` IMMEDIATELY AFTER A `jal` IS THE CALLEE'S RETURN TYPE, caller-side. Retail masks the returned word before testing or storing it, which cc1 only does when the prototype in THIS TU returns a narrow type; declaring it `s32` (which is what the callee's own definition in a neighbouring unit says) drops the `andi` and costs a live register. Round 391 battle/misc11 func_800820A4 measured 96.13% with `extern s32 func_8008C4A8(u8)` and 96.88% with `extern u8 func_8008C4A8(u8)`, the only change being the restored mask. Two TUs may disagree - the definition in misc15.c parks it as `s32` - so read the mask at the call site, not the callee's own return type.
- id: e-a-u16-parameter-that | keywords: extension, narrow, andi, u_short, prototype, declaration, K&R, callee
  **A u_short PARAMETER whose argument retail does NOT mask means the caller's TU had no prototype in scope, and nothing in C with the real definition visible can reproduce it.** round 389 shop_menu/main/misc func_801D05BC (407 insns): retail loads its 0x32-initialised stack giv straight into `$a2` for `ShopMenuSetVertices(..., 0x32 + FONT_LETTER_HEIGHT*i, ...)` while every compile with the u_short prototype visible must emit `andi a2,0xFFFF`, which costs one extra live value and shifts the whole body (93.72%, 1632 bytes vs retail's 1628). Proof of the mechanism, not a theory: an `extern void ShopMenuSetVertices();` line (no prototype) before the function takes it to 99.97% with retail's exact 1628 bytes, and changing the params to `int` measures identically - the prototype's truncation IS the whole first delta. The honest ceiling is therefore one instruction over retail, and the neighbour func_801CDD14 (parked 97.44%, same unit) reports the same missing andi, so treat "retail passes an unmasked word to a narrow parameter defined earlier in the same TU" as a declared wall, not an unexplored shape.

- id: e-kr-declaration-removes-caller | keywords: extension, narrow, sll, sra, prototype, K&R, stack, argument
  **THE K&R-DECLARATION WALL HAS A SIGN-EXTENDED SIBLING: an `s16` parameter passed as a 5th STACK argument costs `sll`+`sra`+the store, and a prototype-less declaration removes all of it** (round 394, world_map/main/misc14 func_80095CD4: 98.72% at 684 bytes with the prototype in scope, 99.93% at retail's exact 676 bytes / 169 instructions with `extern s32 Func();` added). Retail's call tail is `lw t0,0x60(sp)` then, in the jal delay slot, `sw t0,0x10(sp)` - a raw word copy, so its TU had no prototype for the callee. With the callee's own `s16 kind` prototype visible cc1 must narrow and emits `lw t0 / sll v0,t0,0x10 / sra v0,v0,0x10 / sw v0,0x10(sp)`: two instructions and 8 bytes too many, which can never link. Widening the CALLEE to `s32` buys the same 8 bytes but breaks the callee's own `lh` of that argument, so when both functions live in one TU the K&R line is the only spelling that keeps both. Declaring the CALLER's parameter `s16` instead does NOT work: cc1 spills the HImode parameter to a fresh stack slot (`lhu` at entry, `sh` into the frame) and re-extends at the call, 692 bytes / 97.05%. Same family as e-a-u16-parameter-that (which covers the `andi` masking case).

- id: e-byte-round-trip-needs | keywords: extension, narrow, u8, andi, sb, store, order, cse
  **A BYTE GLOBAL THAT IS STORED AND THEN READ BACK MUST BE ROUND-TRIPPED THROUGH A `u8` LOCAL, or cc1 substitutes the SImode value it already holds and you lose both the store's POSITION and the `andi 0xFF`** (round 397, battling/main/misc12 func_80088E90, 91.13 -> 96.84, 808 -> 812 bytes of retail's 816, still parked). Retail's loop head is `sb a0,g_BattleDrawBufferIndex` right after the display-slot address is formed, then `andi a0,a0,0xFF` for the slot index. Writing `g_BattleDrawBufferIndex = (drawFlags + 1) & 1;` and then indexing `pBuf[g_BattleDrawBufferIndex]` lets cse replace the byte load with the SImode value (it knows the masked range), which DROPS the andi and lets sched2 sink the `sb` below the `g_BattleDisplaySlot` store. `u8 drawIndex = (drawFlags + 1) & 1; g_BattleDrawBufferIndex = drawIndex; ... pBuf[drawIndex]` keeps the value QImode: the `sb` lands in retail's place and the index use zero-extends into retail's `andi a0,a0,0xFF`. Same mechanism as e-andi-ffff-kept-by in the other direction: the value's MODE, not the expression, decides whether the zero-extension survives.
- id: e-narrow-conversion-folds-in-a-single-pred-block | keywords: andi, narrow, conversion, cse, single-predecessor, could-not, call argument
  **A NARROW CONVERSION OF A CONSTANT-VALUED LOCAL ON A CALL ARGUMENT SURVIVES AT EVERY JOIN-POINT CALL SITE AND IS FOLDED AT THE ONE SITE WHOSE BLOCK HAS A SINGLE PREDECESSOR** (round 414, menu/main/misc12 func_801E8F60, parked 99.61% at retail's exact 612 bytes / 153 insns under XenoMenu260). Retail has seven `andi a1,s1,0xFF` argument conversions of a local that only ever holds 2 or 3 - six inside the loop heads and one for the trailing call that sits on the last loop's `bnez` fall-through. Every spelling of that body emits the six and drops the seventh as `move a1,s1`: the s32 local with a ternary, a `u8`/`u_char` local, and `mode = 2; if (arg1) mode = 3;` all score 99.61% with that one instruction differing, three 3-minute permuter runs (rounds 318, 399, 414) are PERMUTER_NONE base=200 best=200, and 2.7.2 / 2.7.2-cdk score 99.15. So a MISSING narrow conversion on a function's LAST call after a loop is a cse value-fold, not a type question: cse's table is only cleared at a join, so restoring the mask needs a second predecessor for that block (a branch retail does not have). Park it instead of re-typing the local.
Round 427 corrected the mechanism above with a 15-variant `cc1_probe` on a standalone snippet (one 2-trip loop plus a trailing call, which folds exactly like the body), and the answer is neither cse nor the predecessor count: the fold is COMBINE's constant-value record on the source local, and it happens in EVERY block that is not a loop body. Evidence, all measured on that snippet: setting the local from an unknown value (`mode = g_ProbeK;`, same code shape) keeps the `andi` at every site, so the known 2/3 is the trigger; every spelling that still emits `li s1,2`/`li s1,3` folds the trailing call - `u_char mode`, `if`/`else` instead of the ternary, an `(u_char)` cast, `mode & 0xFF`, a `u_char` or an `s32` copy (`shade = mode;`) immediately before the call, a QI-then-SI double copy, and `for (;;) { call; break; }` (which gcc dissolves to straight-line); two straight-line calls in a row BOTH fold, and a call in an `if` body after a loop folds too. A JOIN does not preserve it either: identical trailing calls in both arms of an `if` survive cross-jumping as one label reached by fall-through and by `j`, and that call still comes out `move a1,s1`, so the old "needs a second predecessor" conclusion is wrong. The mask survives only INSIDE a loop body - a 1-iteration `for (i = 0; i < 1; i++)` rotates into a `blez` loop and keeps the `andi`, at the cost of the loop's branch (which retail does not have). So a missing narrow conversion on a trailing call after a loop is a combine constant-fold wall with no C spelling: probe `mode = <unknown global>;` once to confirm the value is the trigger, then park it. Fourth permuter run skipped deliberately (three previous runs from this same live body, base=200 best=200). A fourth run was finally made in a later lane and is also `RESULT PERMUTER_NONE base=200 best=200` over 1439 iterations, so treat this body as fully spent: do not re-open it without a new compiler.
- id: a-nested-glyph-dimension-strength | keywords: loop, strength, reduce, array, nested, flat, stride, iv
  **A NESTED ARRAY OF PAIRS MUST BE FLAT IN THE SOURCE: `glyphs[j][ctx]` makes cc1 strength-reduce j by the inner stride into a register incremented in the latch (`li s1,0x50` + `addiu s1,s1,80`), where retail's `glyph prim j of context ctx` is a FLAT index whose scale is applied to the whole `2 * j + ctx`** (round 400, menu/main/misc4_260 func_801CF8D8, 78.87 -> 79.94 -> 91.32). Retail: `sll v1,j,1 / addu v1,v1,ctx / sll a1,v1,2 / addu a1,a1,v1 / sll a1,a1,3 / addiu a1,a1,0x50` - the field offset is added AFTER the x40 scale, which is gcc's struct-field form only when the index expression is flat. Declaring the block `POLY_FT4 glyphs[4]` and indexing `glyphs[2 * j + ctx]` reproduces it; `POLY_FT4 glyphs[2][2]` with `glyphs[j][ctx]` emits the 80-byte induction variable instead.

- id: e-u16-range-test-add | keywords: extension, narrow, u16, andi, addu, li, range, short
  **A `(u16)(x + 0xFFFF) < 2` RANGE TEST FOLDS TO `addiu -1` UNLESS THE SUM IS A NAMED 32-BIT LOCAL, AND THE `lhu` COMES FROM THE FIELD'S TYPE** (round 404, battle/main/misc22 func_800A48EC, 98.38 -> 100.00 at retail's exact 148 insns / 592 bytes). Retail's test is five instructions - `lhu v0,0x4A(s1) / li t0,0xFFFF / addu v0,v0,t0 / andi v0,v0,0xFFFF / sltiu v0,v0,2`; the one-line spelling lets cc1 2.6.0 prove the mask is redundant for a `< 2` compare and emit `lhu / nop / addiu v0,v0,-1 / sltiu`, one instruction SHORT, and a body one instruction short can never link (588 bytes, 98.38). `u_int stateMinusOne; stateMinusOne = pSkeleton->state + 0xFFFF; if ((u16)stateMinusOne < 2)` reproduces retail exactly (s32 and u_int both break the fold the same way, 148 insns / 592 bytes). The load width is a separate decision: the struct field must be declared `u16` so the test's load is `lhu`, and the later uses retail reads with `lh` need `(s16)pSkeleton->state` at each of them - with the field `s16` the range-test load comes out `lh` and the body sits at 99.59.
    - id: c-a-pointer-index-distributes-the-addend | keywords: addiu, addu, jal, delay, constant, fold, pointer, index, base
      **A POOL OF `POLY_FT4` REACHED THROUGH `(POLY_FT4 *)base + (idx + K)` DISTRIBUTES K ONTO THE PRODUCT AND PUTS THE BASE ADD IN THE `jal` DELAY SLOT, WHERE EVERY BYTE-OFFSET SPELLING FOLDS K ONTO THE BASE INSTEAD** (round 406, battle/main/misc16_r2 func_80090310, 81.85 -> 98.70 at retail's exact 400 bytes / 100 instructions, XenoBattle260). Retail: `sll a0,v1,2 / addu a0,a0,v1 / sll a0,a0,3 / addiu a0,a0,0x140 / jal / addu a0,a0,t0`. Measured on the function: `(POLY_FT4 *)(prim * 0x28 + 0x140 + (s32)pBase)` gives `addiu v0,t0,320 / addu a0,a0,v0`; the same sum in an `ofs` local gives the CHAIN in `v0` with `addiu a0,v0,320` because cc1 then defers the pool load past the multiply (the local alone reproduces retail's chain in a `cc1_probe`, so the difference is the surrounding block, not the local); `pPrims + prim + 8` puts `addiu a0,a0,320` in the jal slot. Only the pointer-plus-parenthesised-index form gives the chain in the argument register AND the base add last. The palette block of the same function must stay the byte-arithmetic expression DUPLICATED in both `if` arms - that duplication is what reproduces retail's repeated `prim * 5` / palette-load block. Residual after all of that: one v0/v1 mirror of the `prim` and `prim * 5` pseudos inside those arms (not moved by `do { } while (0)` around each arm's store, `((s32 *)g_BattlePrimStartIndex)[0]`, a POLY_FT4 local, or statement/declaration order).

- id: e-u16-cast-on-the-sum | keywords: andi, mask, u16, cast, and, truncation, bounded, loop
  **A `& 0xFFF8`-STYLE MASK ON AN ARGUMENT SUM WHOSE RANGE cc1 CAN BOUND IS EMITTED TWICE - `li t0,-8` + `and` for the C mask AND a second `andi` for the narrow parameter's truncation - AND PUTTING THE `(u16)` CAST ON THE SUM COLLAPSES THEM INTO RETAIL'S SINGLE `andi`** (round 406, menu/main/misc8_r2 func_801DA5BC, 95.44 -> 97.17, 1008 vs retail 1004 bytes; 4 of the 5 surplus instructions). Retail has `addiu a1,s1,0x28 / andi a1,a1,0xfff8` with a `u16` parameter in prototype scope. Inside `for (i = 0; i < 0x10; i++)` with `s32 x = (i % 2) * 0x88;` cc1 proves `x + 0x28` stays under 16 bits, so it rewrites the C's `& 0xFFF8` to `& 0xFFFFFFF8` (one `and`, no zero-extension) and then needs the truncation as a separate `andi 0xfff8`. `(u16)(x + 0x28) & 0xFFF8` gives the single andi, and so does a `u16` local holding the SUM (`u16 v = x + 0x28; ... v & 0xFFF8`); a `u16` local holding `x` does NOT - the subreg is seen through and the range proof survives. Probed with `cc1_probe.sh` on a standalone loop: the same expression in a function whose index is an unbounded parameter already emits one andi, which is why the same spelling measures differently in the unit and in a snippet. Companion measurement from the same body: `(i / 2) * 0x10 | 0xE` (not `+ 0xE`) is what makes cc1 emit retail's `ori s0,s0,0xe`, and the `andi s0,s0,0xffff` truncation stays 0xffff - the or form also kept the mask wide, where `+` narrowed it to 0xfffe in the snippet.
  Round 412 re-measured it on a second shape - `battle/main/misc16_r2` func_80090C44, four call sites of `(d * 8 + 0x78) & 0xF8` with `u16 d;` in the unit's gcc-2.6.0: the last two sites alone get the doubled mask (`sll`/`addiu`/`and` with `li $sN,-8`, then the source's `andi 0xf8`), the `-8` constant then takes the register that held the argument-derived offset (`li $16,-8`) and forces a `move $s4,$s0` copy of that value plus its save/restore, 148 insns / 592 bytes. `(u16)(d * 8 + 0x78) & 0xF8` at all four sites collapses it to the single `andi` - 145 insns / 580 bytes (retail 142 / 568) - so the cast closes the size defect but not by itself the match, because the copy survives at 76.39% vs the 77.70% parked body. Measured with cc1_probe.sh on the four blocks alone, so it is cc1's decision, not the surrounding body's. Round 427: apply the cast ONLY at the sites that actually show the doubled mask (the two `D_800CCD3A` ones) - the first two sites already had a single `andi`, and casting them too costs 1.4% of diff alignment (76.39 all four vs 78.83 on the two, 580 bytes / 145 insns both). The `move s4,s0` copy survives either way, so it is not caused by the `-8` constant taking that register.

- id: a-signed-constant-store-to-a | keywords: andi, lbu, lhu, narrow, extension, ori, addiu, signed, store, constant
  **A NARROW STORE OF A CONSTANT THAT DOES NOT FIT THE NARROW TYPE IS `li`+`ori` WHEN THE TARGET IS UNSIGNED AND `li`+`addiu` WHEN IT IS SIGNED, SO RETAIL'S `addiu v0,zero,-0x8000` FOR A HALFWORD STORE MEANS THE LVALUE WAS `s16 *`** (round 411, battling/main/misc10 func_800868E0, 85.02 -> 86.50 on the same body). The body writes a CLUT's STP bit into a TIM's clut data; `u16 *pClut; pClut[1] = 0x8000;` compiles to `li v0,0x8000` (`ori`) + `sh`, while `s16 *pClut; pClut[1] = -0x8000;` gives retail's `addiu v0,zero,-32768` + `sh` for zero other changes. Suspect the pointer's signedness (not the number's spelling) whenever one `sh`/`sb` of a constant is the only byte difference in an otherwise identical body.
- id: a-0x28-byte-struct-assignment | keywords: struct, block move, copy, loop, unrolled, tail, polylib, assignment
  **gcc 2.7.2 EXPANDS A 0x28-BYTE STRUCT ASSIGNMENT AS A TWO-ITERATION 16-BYTE LOOP PLUS AN 8-BYTE TAIL, SO RETAIL'S `lw x4 / sw x4 / addiu / bne / addiu` COPY BLOCK IS A PLAIN `a[i] = a[i-1]` AND MUST NOT BE HAND-UNROLLED OR WRITTEN AS A C LOOP** (round 411, battling/main/misc10 func_800868E0: six such copies, and with all six present the body is instruction-identical to retail). Two consequences measured there: an 8-byte assignment (e.g. `D_80095918[1] = D_80095918[0]` of `DR_TPAGE`, which is tag + `code[1]` = 8 bytes) is a fully unrolled two-word copy with ABSOLUTE `lui $at` stores, and a park note that counts copies can be wrong - the previous 71.59% park claimed "all five copies" and had four, which is 96 of its 120 missing bytes, so count the loops in the `.s` against the assignments before accepting a percentage.

- id: e-a-byte-parameter-compared | keywords: andi, u-char, parameter, compare, prototype, callback, mask
  **A `u_char` PARAMETER COMPARED TO A CONSTANT COSTS AN `andi r,r,0xff` THAT RETAIL OFTEN DOES NOT HAVE, so a stub whose first two words are `li v0,1 / bne a0,v0` with no mask has an `s32` parameter - even when the function is registered through a `void (*)(u_char, u_char*)` SDK typedef** (round 412, world_map/main/misc15 func_80096C0C: `u_char mode` measures the `andi` and 0x18 frame, `s32 mode` is retail's 0x20 frame and 100.00%, 780 bytes). cc1 2.7.2 zero-extends before comparing because the high half of an incoming narrow argument is opaque to it; an `int` parameter is already a full word per o32. The price is that `CdReadyCallback(func_80096C0C)` then passes an incompatible pointer type (a warning, not an error - no `-Werror` anywhere in gears.toml), and the function's `extern` line in its own `.c` must carry the `s32` signature or cc1 2.7.2 rejects the conflicting definition.

- id: e-narrowing-an-s32-load-that-feeds | keywords: lhu, lw, narrow, sh, rect, compiler, version, 2.6.0, preset
  **AN `s32` ARRAY ELEMENT STORED STRAIGHT INTO A `s16` FIELD IS `lhu` UNDER gcc 2.6.0 AND `lw` UNDER 2.7.2, so a run of `lhu` readbacks with the right register shape is a 2.6.0 signature - not a narrower local** (round 426, battle/main/misc3 func_80078310, parked 81.38% / 484 bytes on the unit's Default preset vs 86.57 on gcc-2.6.0 for the identical body). Retail reads a `s32 metadata[18]` row - six consecutive s32 the six `ResourceHelperGetTexCoords` out-parameters land in - back into `RECT.x/y` as `addu v0,sp,s0 / lhu v0,0x40(v0) / nop / sh v0,0(v1)`, and 2.6.0 emits that byte for byte where 2.7.2 emits `lw`. The struct view that looks tempting (`u16 clutX` with pad halfwords to keep the 4-byte stride) is WRONG and measures 60.00: the out-parameters are `s32 *`, so the row is plain s32 and the narrowing is cc1's. Use this as a free preset screen before typing a narrow struct: `tools/scripts/try_flags.sh <unit> <Func> --gcc gcc-2.6.0-psx` moves 4+ instructions here and costs one compile. Companion fact from the same function: retail can be 2.6.0 in the loads AND carry a giv 2.6.0 does not make (a separate register holding `i-1` for `g_BattleActorCharIds[i]`, the `+1` folded into `%hi(sym+1)`, which spills the sixth out-pointer and grows the frame 0xC0 -> 0xC8), so the preset question and the last register are separate - settle the preset first with `--all` (every matched function must stay 100.00) and only then re-type for the giv.

- id: a-word-spill-means-a-32-bit-parameter | keywords: lhu, lbu, sh, sw, narrow, parameter, width, spill
  **A PARAMETER RETAIL SPILLS WITH `sw` AND RELOADS WITH `lw` IS 32-BIT EVEN WHERE THE BODY ONLY EVER USES IT AS A HALFWORD - a `u16` parameter homes with `sh` and every read becomes `lhu`, so the spill width alone decides the type** (round 431, field/main/misc8 func_80083288, 75.91 -> 78.75 and 446 -> 449 insns against retail's 451, Default preset).
