Retail stores `sw a2,0x18(sp)` / `sw a3,0x20(sp)` in the prologue, reads them back with `lw` for `(refX << 16) + refZ` and *still* emits `lhu 0x18(sp)` / `sh 0x34(s3)` for the `planePoint.vx/vz = refX/refZ` pair, which reads like two types over one slot; probing the unit's own cc1 settles it - an addressable `u16` parameter homes with `sh` and a `u16` local reads back `lhu`, so only `int` parameters give retail's `sw`/`lw`, and the loop's `lhu 0x18(sp)` / `sh 0x34(s3)` pair came out of the same `int` body, so no second u16 variable is needed to explain retail's two widths. Probe recipe: `tools/scripts/cc1_probe.sh <unit> <snippet.c>` with the parameter's address taken, one compile per candidate type.

- id: e-a-boolean-is-qimode-inside-a-ternary-arm | keywords: andi, sltiu, srl, xori, boolean, ternary, move, comparison
  **A 0/1 BOOLEAN IS `andi` + `sltiu` WHEN IT IS ASSIGNED STRAIGHT TO AN `int` VARIABLE, BUT `srl` + `xori` + `andi` (QImode) WHEN THE SAME EXPRESSION SITS IN A TERNARY ARM - so read the QImode shape as "this comparison was a COND_EXPR arm", not as "one statement instead of two"** (round 435, menu/main/misc8_r2 func_801DA9A8, parked 98.08% at 1664 of retail's 1668 bytes / 416 of 417 insns, XenoMenu260 = gcc-2.6.0). Retail's row selector is `andi v0,v1,0x1000 / sltiu v0,v0,1 / move s1,v0` (SImode, the result copied into the caller-visible variable) reached by `if (flags & 0x4000) row = 2; else { row = flags & 0x1000; row = !row; }`; the same logic written as one ternary `row = (flags & 0x4000) ? 2 : (flags & 0x1000) == 0;` measures 96.78 and emits `srl v0,v1,0xc / xori v0,v0,0x1 / andi s4,v0,0x1`, and `row = !row` vs `row = row == 0` is byte-identical (98.08 both). Two spellings measured dead on the same body: a block-scoped `s32 rowFlags = flags & 0x1000; row = rowFlags == 0;` is 97.52 / 1660 / 415 (cc1 then drops both andi copies and swaps two callee-saved), and the preset screen (2.7.2 97.47, 2.7.2-cdk does not compile) leaves 2.6.0 in place. The residual is the ONE `move` that puts the boolean into a second pseudo; `-dg` on the body shows the callee-saved hand-out is rowAlt -> $s0, arg0 -> $s1, 0x80 -> $s2, row -> $s3, i.e. row is processed last because it has one ref fewer than retail's, so the copy and the register rotation are the same defect.

- id: e-a-char-variable-cast-of-a-comparison-goes-qimode-so-route-it-through-an-s32 | keywords: andi, sltiu, srl, xori, boolean, move, char, u8, intermediate, qimode
  **THE ROUND-435 QIMODE RESIDUAL ON THE SAME BODY WAS THE DESTINATION'S WIDTH, NOT A TERNARY: ASSIGNING A COMPARISON STRAIGHT TO A `u_char` MAKES cc1 COMPARE IN QImode (`srl`/`xori`/`andi`), AND ROUTING IT THROUGH AN `s32` LOCAL RECOVERS RETAIL'S SImode PAIR** (round 448, menu/main/misc8_r2 func_801DA9A8, 98.08 -> MATCH at retail's exact 1668 bytes / 417 insns, XenoMenu260 = gcc-2.6.0). `row = (flags & 0x1000) == 0;` with `u8 row` emits `srl v0,v1,0xc / xori v0,v0,0x1 / andi s1,v0,0x1` (98.06 at the right size); `rowFlags = flags & 0x1000; row = rowFlags == 0;` with `s32 rowFlags` at function scope emits retail's `andi v0,v1,0x1000 / sltiu v0,v0,1 / move s1,v0` and the function matches. The `u8 row` itself was the other half of the fix: retail masks the argument at the call with `andi s1,s1,0xff` (a char value, unbounded) where an `s32 row` with arms 0..2 lets cc1 narrow with `andi v0,s3,0x3` - read that mask as the declaration's type, exactly as `e-decrement`-style width levers say. So on a boolean-assignment residual: first fix the destination's declared width from the mask retail emits, then give the comparison an `s32` temp if retail still shows `andi`+`sltiu` (the temp is the only spelling that keeps SImode; the note in the round-435 lever that a block-scoped `s32 rowFlags` is 97.52 was measured with an `s32 row`, not this body).
- id: s32-intermediate-forces-lh-where-the-store-narrows-to-lhu | keywords: lh, lhu, andi, lbu, narrow, extension, s16, intermediate, position
  **A SIGNED HALFWORD LOAD WHOSE ONLY USE IS A HALFWORD STORE COMES OUT `lhu`; ROUTING THE SAME READ THROUGH AN `s32` LOCAL RECOVERS RETAIL'S `lh`** (round 440, field/main/misc8 func_80081C54: retail `lh v1,0x22(t0)` / `sh v1,%lo(...)(at)` for three 16.16 position halves read as `((s16*)&pActor->position)[1]`, the direct spelling emits `lhu` because cc1 narrows the load to HImode once the store truncates it). `s32 posX; ... posX = ((s16*)&pActor->position)[1]; D_x[i].positionX = posX;` gives retail's `lh` for all three - and cost 1 point on that body (92.57 -> 91.59) because three values that were dead immediately become live across the group and rotate the s-block, so measure it rather than assuming a pure win. The companion fact from the same body: `s32` and `int` are the same type here, so the intermediate must be a real local, not a cast.

- id: b-two-adjacent-coordinate-stores-are-one-packed-libgpu-macro | keywords: store, andi, lbu, narrow, width, packed, setXY0, setUV0, sprite
  **TWO ADJACENT HALFWORD/byte FIELD STORES OF CONSTANTS NEVER MERGE IN 2.7.2, BUT RETAIL'S SINGLE `sw` / `sh` IS THE PACKED LIBGPU MACRO FORM: write `*(u32 *)&pSprt->x0 = (y0 << 16) | x0;` and `*(u16 *)&pSprt->u0 = (v0 << 8) | u0;` and the constant register, the frame and the whole saved set fall into place** (round 452, battling/main/misc8 func_80080F04, 84.65 -> 98.70% at retail's exact 400 bytes / 100 insns, Default preset). Retail stores `lui v0,0xB40000` / `sw v0,sprt.x0`, `ori s2,0,0x3000` / `sh s2,sprt.u0` - one packed word for x0,y0 and one packed halfword for u0,v0 - while `pSprt->x0 = 0; pSprt->y0 = 0xB4; pSprt->u0 = 0; pSprt->v0 = 0x30;` costs a 0x30 frame instead of 0x28 and two extra callee-saved constants (`li s2,0xb4` / `li s1,0x30`). The repo's `setXY0`/`setUV0` macros in include/psyq/libgpu.h are the comma-separated store form, so the packed spelling has to be written out; the same idiom is already in the unit's matched neighbours (`*(u32 *)&pQuad->x0 = ...`, `*(u16 *)&pDim->sprt.u0 = 0`). Second find on the same body: the sprite pointer must NOT be a local - writing `SetSprt(&pText[n].sprt[0])` at each of the four call sites (instead of `pSprt = &pText[n].sprt[0]`) moved the hand-out from {`$s1`=0x3000, `$s2`=0xD, `$s3`=pointer} to retail's {pointer `$s1`, 0x3000 `$s2`, 0xD `$s3`} with no byte change (84.65 -> 98.10 before the packing edit), i.e. one more instance of `a-second-pointer-alias-carries-the-reads-retail-names` with the pointer variable removed rather than re-partitioned.

## division mfhi/mflo

symptoms: div/mfhi/mflo or mult/srl sequence differs
try: div mfhi mflo mult
- id: f-divisor-argument-original-passes | keywords: division, mfhi, andi, div, move, s32, signed, sll
  A divisor/argument the original passes with a plain move/div and no andi or sll/sra pair wants the narrow SIGNED type (s16), not u8/s32.
- id: f-signed-product-cast-u32 | keywords: division, mfhi, multu, cast, mult, signed, srl
- id: f-mult-operand-order-follows | keywords: mult, operand, order, lbu, clamp, source-order, slti, preset
  **THE `mult` OPERAND ORDER FOLLOWS SOURCE ORDER AND THE LOAD CLUSTER FOLLOWS THE SAME SOURCE ORDER, so a park whose retail `mult v1,v0` is the REVERSE of its load cluster is unreachable from C under gcc 2.7.2** (round 396, battle/main/misc5 func_8007AA60, parked 99.09% at retail's exact 88 bytes / 22 insns; the only differing line is `mult v1,v0` against `mult v0,v1`, i.e. one ENCODING - the product is identical, so objdiff can never close it and the bytes cannot link). Measured: `p->operand2 * pRow[p->operand1]` emits retail's cluster exactly (`lbu idx`, `lbu operand2`, `addu`, `lbu row`) with `mult` in v0,v1; writing the row first (`pRow[...] * p->operand2`, an `u8` local pair for the operands - copy-propagated away - the typed `g_BattleSlotRegs[arg1].byteRegs[i]` form, or a `volatile` read of `p->operand2`) all give 71.05-71.09, where the operand2 `lbu` sinks below the row load and the mult then puts the row value in v0, i.e. the RIGHT operand order with the WRONG cluster. The two are locked together because 2.7.2 expands the multiply's operands in source order. Preset is not the way out: 2.6.0 and 2.7.2-cdk both emit retail's cluster from the row-first body but write `slt` where retail has `slti` (2.6.0 scores 99.55), and `-fschedule-insns`/`-fschedule-insns2` under 2.7.2 leave the stream unchanged (checked in cc1's own output, not with try_flags). A prior permuter run (2259 iterations, base=20 best=20) agrees; round 411 re-measured the same unit and confirmed it from both sides (`s32 mul = p->operand2;` then `pRow[p->operand1] * mul` keeps operand2 in `$v1` and scores 71.09) and a third 3-minute permuter run (1733 iterations) returned base=20 best=20.
  Round 424 measured the last spelling pair on the same function: `u8 imm = p->operand2;` as a statement before a row-first multiply is 71.05 and mirrors the whole allocation (the base `lui/addiu` also moves below the `addu`), i.e. splitting the operand read into its own statement does NOT decouple the cluster from the mult order. Park, do not re-spell.
  A signed product cast to u32 before dividing gives mult + multu/srl; a u32 product gives multu.
  Round 437 CORRECTS the 2.6.0 half of this entry: the claim that 2.6.0 leaves `slt` where retail has `slti`
  is false - it came from the TYPED spelling's reloc addend, not from the compare. `extern u8
  g_BattleSlotByteRegs[][0x40];` + `u8 *pRow = g_BattleSlotByteRegs[arg1];` +
  `s16 v = pRow[p->operand1] * p->operand2; if (v > 0xFF) v = 0xFF; pRow[p->operand1] = v;` compiles
  BYTE-IDENTICALLY to retail under gcc-2.6.0-psx (objdump -dr of the function, relocations and addends
  included, cmp-clean), i.e. 100.00 rather than 99.55, and `try_flags.sh battle/main/misc5 --all --gcc
  gcc-2.6.0-psx` scores every OTHER function of the unit exactly as under Default (all six matched ones
  stay 100.00), so the unit switch buys this function for free. The lesson generalises: a park note's
  "N under 2.6.0, it emits X instead of retail's Y" can be the RELOC FOLD of a typed `g_BattleSlotRegs`
  spelling rather than a codegen difference, so re-dump the candidate object and compare instruction
  text before believing a compiler can't produce retail's stream - and when the address of a bank
  member is a symbol of its own (g_BattleSlotByteRegs = g_BattleSlotRegs+0x30), spelling it as that
  symbol is what closes the last addend.
  Round 427 found the mirror image under 2.6.0 - COMMUTATIVE OPERAND ORDER CAN BE THE ONLY RESIDUAL EVEN WHEN THE CLUSTER AND THE REGISTERS ARE ALREADY RETAIL'S, and it is then not source-controllable either: battle/main/misc19 func_8009A2D4's best 2.6.0 body (313 insns / 1252 bytes, retail's exact size) differs in exactly `mult v1,v0` against retail's `mult v0,v1`, `addu a0,v1,v0` against `addu a0,v0,v1` and `addu v1,v0,a0` against `addu v0,v0,a0`, with the three loads (`lbu v0,87(s0)` / `lhu v1,0(a1)`, `lbu v0,18(s0)`, `lbu v0,34(s0)`) and every destination in retail's register. Measured with NO effect: writing the product row-first (`rowCosts[0] * volatile statBonuses[7]` - cluster and mult byte-identical), writing the sum weapon-first (`weaponData[0].attack + attack` - output byte-identical), and storing the second sum with no local (`pWork->attackAmount = attack + weaponData[2].attack;` fixes that destination but flips the earlier product's load cluster, 21 differing lines instead of 19). So treat an encoding-only commutative swap as 99.74-class ceiling: park it, do not spend spellings.
- id: f-mfhi-mflo-destination-is-the-preset | keywords: division, mfhi, mflo, destination, coalesce, srl, preset, 2.6.0, carve-out
  **A DIVMOD WHOSE ONLY RESIDUAL IS THE `mfhi`/`mflo` DESTINATION IS A PRESET SIGNATURE, NOT A SPELLING**: retail writes the highpart/quotient straight into the register the next instruction consumes (`mfhi a0 / sra a0,a0,5`, `mflo v0 / sh v0,38(a1)`, `mfhi v0 / srl v0,v0,3`), and gcc 2.7.2-psx always inserts a temp (`mfhi a1 / sra a0,a1,5`, `mflo t0 / sh t0,...`, `mfhi t0 / srl v0,t0,3`) - the divmod's md names a fresh pseudo that the allocator does not coalesce with the consumer. Round 414, battle/main/misc19: `func_8009A9D0` is 99.66% (116 bytes, 29/29 insns, the ONLY differing line is that pair) and 100.00 under gcc-2.6.0 (try_flags), while -O3, cdk, `50 > rand() % 100`, `<= 49`, the modulo result in a local and two permuter runs (base=10 best=10) all leave it at 99.66; its near-twin `BattleRollPercentAndSetState38` has retail's `mfhi a0` under 2.6.0 because misc18 is a XenoBattle260 unit, so a twin is only evidence for the md when it shares the preset. The same unit's `func_8009A2D4` (313 insns) carries three of these sites and moves only 99.19 -> 99.52 under 2.6.0. So: count the mfhi/mflo destinations in the diff before writing a spelling, and if they are the residual, screen the unit with `try_flags.sh <unit> --all --gcc gcc-2.6.0-psx` and propose a one-function carve-out (PRESET_260) instead of hunting shapes - and park the body at retail's byte count with the md named.
- id: b-pointer-sum-flips-a-whole-8-store-block | keywords: addu, operand, index-first, store, struct, array, allocno, permuter
  **FOR A STRUCT-ARRAY ELEMENT STORE, `(idx + array)->field` INSTEAD OF `array[idx].field` FLIPS NOT JUST THE `addu` BUT THE WHOLE STATEMENT'S ALLOCATION - and the permuter finds it in one edit** (round 409, menu/main/misc4_260 func_801D02D8, 98.48 -> 100.00 at retail's exact 1660 bytes / 415 insns, gcc 2.6.0). The function's eight `slotHighlightQuads[g_Menu->renderContext].uN/vN` stores each re-read `g_Menu`, its `renderContext` and `pPrimBuffer` (an `lw`/`lw`/`lw` cluster), and with the subscript form every cluster loaded `g_Menu` into `$v1` where retail has `$a1`, cascading into eight permuted blocks (172 differing lines) that no declaration order, operand order or `* 16` / `16 *` swap moved. One statement rewritten as `(g_Menu->renderContext + pPrims->slotHighlightQuads)->u3` took it to 98.70; all eight took it to 100.00. The permuter's only edit was exactly that (RESULT PERMUTER_BETTER base=630 best=540, 1813 iters, 4 min), and its 540 was the one-site body - so when a diff shows the SAME load cluster permuted at N sibling stores of one struct array, try the pointer-sum form at ONE site, re-measure, then apply it to the rest. Same mechanism as `b-index-first-subscript-pointer`, but here it also moves the allocno of the reloaded global, not only the `addu` operands.

- id: f-a-plain-sra-means-the | keywords: division, sra, shift, div, rounding, power-of-two, blocks, srl
  **A POWER-OF-TWO DIVISION THAT RETAIL EMITS AS A BARE `sra` IS A SHIFT IN THE SOURCE: gcc's signed `/32` adds `addiu x,31` / sign-fix instructions that no `>>` produces, so a 2-instruction size excess on the same body is that division, not the allocator** (round 419, slus_006.64/psyq/libgpu_b func_800460A0, 91.29 -> 94.79 in one edit; `blocks = total / 32` also emitted the rounding TWICE because the value feeds two places). Compare against the neighbouring `/2` in the same body: it keeps the full rounding (`srl 31 / addu / sra 1`), so the source did divide by 2 - the two spellings sit side by side and the plain `sra` is the tell. The give-away in a diff is `addiu t,x,32` (or `+31`) next to the shift.

- id: f-dividend-dies-at-the-division | keywords: division, in-place, sra, addu, shift, size, copy, blocks
  **THE ROUND-UP GOES IN PLACE ON THE DIVIDEND'S REGISTER (and the later shift gets a FRESH callee-saved register with a copy) ONLY WHEN THE DIVIDEND IS DEAD AFTER THE DIVISION - so `total / 2` and `total >> 5` on the same variable can never produce it; divide into a `size` local and derive both the block count and the remainder from `size`** (round 420, slus_006.64/psyq/libgpu_b StoreImage func_800462DC, 97.80 -> 99.29 -> 99.94 -> 100.00, PSY-Q preset). Retail is `mflo a2 / addiu v1,a2,1 / srl v0,v1,31 / addu v1,v1,v0 / sra a0,v1,1 / bgtz a0 / sra s0,v1,5 / ... / addu v1,s0,zero / sll v0,v1,4 / subu s0,a0,v0 / addu s4,v1,zero`: the shift reads the ROUNDED value, which is only reachable through `(total/2) >> 4` folded into `t >> 5`, i.e. the source has no second use of `total`. `blocks = size >> 4; rest = size - blocks * 16;` then loses retail's two copies (96.09, 4 bytes short): the closing spelling is the permuter's `rest = size >> 4; rest = size - rest * 16; blocks = size >> 4;` - the SECOND computation of `size >> 4` is a separate pseudo whose register rest then takes, which is what forces `move v1,s0` + `addu s4,v1,zero`. `blocks = rest; rest = ...` coalesces them back (96.09), so the repeat is load-bearing; note it in the source. Companion measured on the same body: the guard must be `if (size <= 0)`, not `if (total / 2 <= 0)`, or the divisor's round-up lands in a fresh `$v0` and every register downstream shifts.

## loop shape and invariant hoisting

- id: an-o-equals-i-times-stride-body-keeps-the-symbol-store-form | keywords: loop, counter, offset, accumul, regalloc, swapped, biv, giv, stride
  **A CLEAR LOOP WHOSE TWO VARIABLES LAND MIRRORED (COUNTER IN `$a2`, BYTE OFFSET IN `$a3`) POINT THE BODY AT ACCUMULATING `o += 6` WHERE RETAIL ASSIGNS `o = i * 6`, AND THE ASSIGNMENT FORM ALSO PRESERVES WHICH OF THE TWO STORE FORMS gcc PICKS: with a NAMED s32 offset retail's stores come out as `lui at,%hi(sym) / addu at,at,o / sh %lo(sym)(at)` (the offset added per store), which the assignment form reproduces exactly, and the offset then takes `$a2` with the counter in `$a3`** (round 452, battle/main/misc13 func_800877E0, 60.72 -> 62.91% at 448 bytes, retail 456: loop 1 went from every line differing by register name to exact). Writing `= i * 6` inside the body instead of `o += 6` makes gcc build the GIV `i*6` and keep it as a scalar because the address is `symbol + o`; the accumulator spelling gets the two BIVs the other way round. The limit found on the same body: the same trick does NOT fix a second loop whose offset also competes with a loaded table pointer (`lw a2,%lo(g_BattleSlotParamTable)` takes `$a2` and pushes the offset to `$t0`) - there the residual is which pseudo wins `$a1`/`$a2`, and a typed struct chain for the same offsets scores WORSE (59.85 / 452), because gcc's emission for the typed form differs.

- id: g-the-rebuild-loop-counter-is-the-shared-index-variable | keywords: loop, counter, callee-saved, swapped, shared, variable, index, live-range
  **WHEN A FUNCTION HAS A REBUILD LOOP AND A LATER/OTHER ARM THAT USES AN INDEX VARIABLE, RETAIL'S CALLEE-SAVED HAND-OUT USES THE SAME REGISTER FOR BOTH - so the loop counter IS that variable, and a fresh `int i` mirrors the pair** (round 443, field/main/misc7 func_80099214, Default preset, 97.91 -> 97.99 with the 12 register-diff lines on the loop gone). `for (walkmesh = 0; walkmesh < g_FieldWalkmeshTriCount - 1; walkmesh++)` with `walkmesh` also used as the walkmesh id in the tail gives retail's `$s1` counter / `$s0` normals-base pair, because one pseudo with disjoint live ranges ranks in global.c as the tail's variable does; a separate `int i` gives `i` `$s0` and the base `$s1`, the mirror. Declaring the counter in the `for()` or as a `while` with `walkmesh++` at the bottom compiles byte-identically, so the lever is the VARIABLE, not the loop syntax. Its residue here is that reorg then has only the result store to fill the `bnez` slot with (loop.c would have had to hoist the `a2 = i` argument copy to leave retail's `addu a2,s1,zero` in the slot), which is worth one instruction and is not reachable from source.


- id: g-two-preheader-inits-are-one-comma-statement | keywords: loop, preheader, counter, init, move, lui, addiu, comma, statement-order
  **A PREHEADER THAT ZEROES THE COUNTER AND THEN FORMS THE WALKED BASE (`move a3,zero` / `lui a2,%hi(g_Table)` / `addiu a2,a2,%lo`) IS ONE `for (i = 0, p = g_Table; ...)` INIT, NOT `p = g_Table;` FOLLOWED BY THE `for`** (round 453, world_map/main/misc10 func_8008A5B8, 90.48 -> 92.63%, XenoWorldMapDiv, 93 insns both: with it the whole body except the prologue is exact, 14 differing lines -> 12; measured in the same edit as dropping a `void* pSprite` temp, so the two together own the 2.15 points and the preheader hunk is the part visibly gone). The two-statement form let cse/sched2 put the base's `lui/addiu` first and the `move a3,zero` after it, while retail has the counter's zeroing first, which is the order a left-to-right comma init forces. Cheap and byte-neutral to try on any loop whose only non-prologue residual is the order of two preheader instructions.

symptoms: loop rotated or not, constant hoisted or reloaded
try: loop hoist invariant rotated
- id: g-which-four-invariants-are-hoisted | keywords: loop, hoist, invariant, callee-saved, frame, rematerialise, saved-set, local
  **THE SAVED SET NAMES WHICH LOOP INVARIANTS RETAIL KEPT, AND A BODY CAN ONLY MATCH IF gcc HOISTS THE SAME FOUR: a park whose calls, frame size and per-iteration instruction stream are already retail's can still be 2 insns short purely because the four callee-saved registers hold different invariants** (round 433, slus_006.64/system/temp3 func_8001B970, re-parked 86.53% / 404 bytes / 101 insns against retail's 424 / 103, Default preset). Retail keeps `$s0` = the byte offset `i`, `$s1` = `pRow = base + i`, `$s2`/`$s3` = `inBuf + 1` / `inBuf` and REMATERIALISES both the odd byte's base (`lui t2,%hi(g_GameState+1)` / `addiu t2,t2,%lo(...)` per row) and the terminator `0xF` (`li t1,0xf` per row); every body tried keeps `i`, the source base, `outBuf`'s address and `0xF` instead, so `inBuf`'s address is recomputed per row into a caller-saved register and the odd base takes a fifth callee-saved. Spelling the odd base as its own pointer local (`u8 *pOdd = (u8 *)&g_GameState + 1;` read `pOdd[i + j]`) does not stop the hoist (84.11% / 420 bytes / 105 insns, six saves) and neither does hoisting the `inBuf`/`inBuf + 1` pairs to pre-loop pointers (identical code: gcc deletes the dead stores). So before hunting an address form, diff the SAVED SET and the two cheapest rematerialisations (`lui/addiu` of a constant address, `li` of a small constant) - if retail rematerialises them and the compile hoists them, the residual is the hoist ranking, and no spelling tried moves it.
- id: g-pointer-locals-mint-a-derived-giv-per-base | keywords: loop, giv, induction, pointer, frame, callee-saved, row, stride
  **THREE ROW POINTERS INCREMENTED PER ITERATION COST gcc ONE DERIVED INDUCTION VARIABLE PER BASE (`pAhead`, `pAhead+4`, `pBehind`, `pBehind+4`, `pDest+4`), WHICH IS TWO CALLEE-SAVED REGISTERS AND A FRAME THAT RETAIL DOES NOT HAVE; spelling the two outer rows as `(pDest + stride)[k]` lets gcc derive them from the `pDest` biv and the frame disappears** (round 430, slus_006.64/system/system func_80034FFC, 58.11 -> 60.59 -> 66.79%, 424 retail insns / 1696 bytes against 421/1684, 413/1652 and 391/1564). Retail's backbone is four increments in the latch (`addu t3,t3,t4` / `addu t1,t1,t4` / `addu t2,t2,t4` / `addu a2,a2,t4` = the three rows plus one `pDest+4` giv), the word accesses stay 0/2/4 immediates off the two row registers, the stride is loop-invariant in `t4`, and the loop needs no callee-saved register at all. With the pointer locals the compile instead gives every base its own +4 giv and the loop no longer fits in caller-saved registers, so the count of `addu <reg>,<reg>,<stride>` in the latch is the thing to read in the diff before trying spellings. The inline form then overshoots the other way: gcc picks the `pDest+4` giv as the loop variable and re-derives `pDest`/`pBehind` from it (`move a2,a0`, a `subu` per use). Recomputing the two row pointers as statements at the top of the body scores 63.23% - between the two.
- id: g-hoist-the-row-pointer-and-the-id-pointer | keywords: loop, hoist, invariant, index, pointer-walk, scale, andi, induction, mult, array
  **A LOOP-INVARIANT SCALED BASE THAT RETAIL KEEPS IN A SAVED REGISTER (`fp = arg0*0x48`, then `addu v0,fp,%lo(ids)` + `addu v0,v0,i`) IS NOT RE-FORMED PER ITERATION IF THE SOURCE HOLDS THE ELEMENT POINTER: `u16 *pRows = &g_BattleCommandRows[arg0 * 0x24]` read as `pRows[i]` gives retail's walked `s3` (`la` once, `lh/lhu 0(s3)`, `addiu s3,s3,2` in the latch), and the same for the sibling index** (round 428, battle/main/misc13 func_80085618, park 60.72 -> 63.97 with the row pointer and 64.20% / 1140 bytes with `u8 *pCmd = &g_BattleActorCommandIds[arg0 * 0x48]` + `switch (pCmd[i])`, Default preset; retail 1196 bytes / 299 insns, still 14 insns short). Indexing the same array inline makes cc1 re-form `(u8)arg0 * 0x48` from an `andi 0xff` at EVERY site (5 insns for the id read, 2 per row read - 20+ insns over ten sites) even though the identical scaled value was computed in the prologue, because the loop's expression reaches it through a fresh QImode pseudo. Generalises to any `arr[invariant + i]` read in many arms of a switch: hoist the element pointer, not the offset. Watch the trade: the pointer walk gives ONE induction variable where retail may have TWO (the offset form), so expect the frame and saved set to shift - re-read retail's latch before crediting the gain.
- id: g-index-form-keeps-the-divisor-in-the-loop | keywords: loop, hoist, division, divisor, index, pointer-walk, frame, spill
  **A LOOP-INVARIANT DIVISOR THAT RETAIL RE-COMPUTES EVERY ITERATION (`srl/addu/sra` right before a variable `div`) IS KEPT IN THE LOOP BY INDEXING THE ARRAY AT THE TOP OF THE BODY INSTEAD OF WALKING A POINTER - and with it go the spill, the +8 frame and the whole saved-register block** (round 427, battling/main/misc5 func_8007DC74, 78.83 -> 80.47 with the frame going 0x70 -> retail's 0x68 and the saved set 0x48.. -> retail's 0x40..). Retail's spray loop divides by `dy / 2` at the `div`; the pointer-walk body (`for (i = 0; i < N; i++, pSlot++)`, everything reachable through the walking pointer) made cc1 hoist the shift pair into the preheader, allocate it a stack slot at sp+0x40 and push every saved register 8 bytes up. Writing `pSlot = &((BattleSpriteSlot *)g_BattleSpriteSlots)[i];` as the first statement of the body keeps the divisor in the loop and reproduces retail's frame and epilogue exactly, and it also pulls the `spread - (len2 >> 31)` correction back into the `rand()` delay slot where retail has it. The trade it makes is the induction pair: the index form gives ONE biv (a byte offset counted in the latch, `s1 = base + offset` formed once) where retail keeps TWO (base and base+2, both incremented), so a body can be at retail's frame and still 2 instructions short - use this lever for the frame/hoist half, then look for the second induction variable separately. Measured worse on the same function: the correction inlined at its four uses (74.59, 236 insns) and an early `pBase` local with `&pBase[i]` (79.81, 230 insns, it reloads the table base after the intervening calls).
- id: g-a-named-offset-local-pins-the-giv | keywords: giv, offset, induction, cse, fold, symbol, local, loop
  **NAMING ONE OF A LOOP'S TWO BYTE-OFFSET SUBEXPRESSIONS IN ITS OWN LOCAL IS WHAT PINS THE GIV PAIR cse OTHERWISE RE-FOLDS INTO ONE BASE PLUS A SYMBOL ADDEND** (round 419, battle/main/misc3 func_8007819C, 98.39 -> 100.00 at retail's exact 372 bytes / 93 insns, Default preset). Retail keeps two offset induction variables, A = 0x60+0xC0k (used by both symbol stores `D_800D36C0 + A` / `g_BattleTextSlotBuffers + A` and by the RECT copy destination `slotRects + A`) and B = 0xC0k (used only by the `&slotRects[i*0x60] - 0x50` argument), and the two stores show the symbol re-formed each iteration (`lui at,%hi(D_800D36C0); addu at,at,A; sw v0,0(at)`). Every spelling that computed the store offset inline from the loop index got it canonicalised as `symbol+0x60 + B` (relocation `D_800D3720`, `sw v0,96(at)`), so A and B traded `$s3`/`$s0` and the body sat at 98.39 with retail's exact size and instruction count; writing the OTHER offset into a named local before its two uses (`offPrev = i * sizeof(Row);`) restores retail's pair and the whole function matches (permuter found it in 74 iterations, base 420 best 0). Measured on the same body: the loop rewritten as a typed struct-array index (`g_BattleTextSlotRects[i].pBuf`, `[i + 1].pBuf`, `[i].rect`) is 90.23% / 101 insns because cc1 emits `lui/addu` per `arr[i]` access instead of strength-reducing it, and giving BOTH offsets their own explicit incremented locals (init 0x60 and 0, `+= 0xC0` at the bottom) is 90.00% / 95 insns because cse then re-derives the copy destination from the rect pointer. Companion negative from the same round: a typed `POLY_FT4 *p` walk with `->` members does NOT stop loop.c minting a field giv (func_80076D58 measures 92.31% identically to the `u8 *` + byte-offset form), so g-index-form-makes-the-field-givs does not transfer to a do-while whose cursor is a pointer.
- id: g-giv-preheader-order-follows-the-use | keywords: loop, giv, preheader, hoist, strength, order, accumulator, inline
  **A STRENGTH-REDUCED BASE THAT RETAIL HOISTS INTO THE PREHEADER IN A GIVEN ORDER COMES OUT IN RETAIL'S ORDER ONLY WHEN THE SCALED EXPRESSION SITS INLINE AT ITS USE (a call argument, with the invariant part a plain scalar set before the loop) - hoisting it into a source local at the top of the loop body records that giv at the wrong point and swaps the preheader `li` pairs** (round 418, shop_menu/main/misc func_801CEB3C, 99.92 -> 100.00 at retail's exact 1892 bytes / 473 insns, XenoLibrary preset; the park had been measured six ways over two rounds and two permuter runs). Retail's preheader is `li s8,0x1000 / li s7,0x3020 / lui+lw g_Menu / li s6,0x49 / lw pShop / li s5,0x27B0`; the park's `x = 0x49 + j * 0x1A;` at the top of the loop body plus `for (k = 0, col = x; k < 3; )` + `col += 8` gave s6=0x27B0 and s5=0x49 (six instructions, the two `move s1,sN` copies, one `addu`, two latch addius and the `li` pair). The closing spelling is the one the matched twin GearShopMenuBuildPurchaseItemDetails uses (gear_shop_menu/main/render.c): `x = 0x49;` before the outer loop and, in the func call's argument list, `(j * FONT_LETTER_HEIGHT * 2) + x + (k * MENU_DIGIT_SPACING)` - so cc1 derives TWO givs from the one use, the outer one (`li 0x49`, `addiu 0x1A` in the outer latch) recorded where cse creates it, and the inner one (`addu s1,s6,zero` at loop entry, `addiu s1,s1,8` in the latch) exactly as retail has them. Generalisable: read the retail preheader as giv-recording order = source order of the USES, and do not introduce a source accumulator or a pre-loop `x = base + j*stride` assignment for a value the original likely wrote inline.
- id: g-scaled-record-index-is-a-giv | keywords: loop, giv, strength, reduce, multiply, sizeof, index, allocator
  **A SCALED RECORD INDEX IN A HOT INNER LOOP IS STRENGTH-REDUCED INTO ITS OWN INDUCTION REGISTER WHATEVER THE SPELLING, AND THAT ONE REGISTER IS WHAT SHIFTS THE WHOLE ALLOCATION** (round 406, battle/main/misc29 func_800B7424, parked 73.66% at 1092 bytes / 273 insns against retail's 1100 / 275 under XenoBattleTailCdk0). Retail recomputes the sprite address every iteration - `sll v0,fp,0x5 / subu v0,v0,fp / sll v0,v0,0x2` (column * 0x7C) then `addu v1,pWork+blockOffset / +rowBytes / addu s2,v1,v0` - and keeps `radius` in a STACK SLOT (0x48) while `$fp` holds the column counter; cc1 2.7.2-cdk instead makes `col * 0x7C` a mult giv (`addiu sN,sN,124` in the latch), spills the column counter to make room, keeps `radius` in `$fp`, and then every record address and every store through it differs (269 of 550 lines). `col * sizeof(BattleEffectSprite)` and `&((BattleEffectSprite *)base)[col]` compile to the SAME code, so a typed index is not the escape. Rebuilding the body with the per-row z snapshots as separate locals (rowOffset/passOffset) does reproduce retail's frame (0xA0) and its 0x58/0x5C/0x60 slots exactly but scores worse (1080 bytes / 270 insns / 68.79%), so slot-for-slot agreement is not progress here. Read this before spending a lane on a large loop whose only residual is "every address in the inner loop": compare the latch of the inner loop for an `addiu sN,sN,<stride>` first.
 Round 429 addendum: the 73.66% park this entry cites is NOT a usable base - it called rand() twice where retail calls once, swapped the two pass constants (retail's `addiu v0,s7,-0x9B` is the pass == 0 path, `-0x95` the pass != 0 one) and returned void where retail's tail is `lw v0,0xA0(sp)`. A body written from m2c's own slot decomposition - which reports retail's eleven slots verbatim, 0x48 radius, 0x4C row, 0x50 pass, 0x54 rowCoord, 0x58/0x5C the row z snapshots, 0x60 colCoord, 0x64 rowBytes, 0x68/0x6C the two z accumulators, 0x70 blockOffset - sits at 276 insns / 1104 bytes / 71.49%, retail's instruction list one instruction over with the whole allocation displaced. Dropping the rowCoord local and writing `row << 4` at its three uses measures 269 / 1076 / 78.03%: the fuzzy number RISES while the body goes 24 bytes short, so it is not progress - keep the slot. g-qimode-index-blocks-the-mult-giv's test says this one is unreachable from C: retail has neither an `andi` on the column counter nor an `sltiu` on its `!= 20` guard, so the source counter is s32 and the mult giv cannot be dodged. Preset screened on the same body over the whole unit: gcc 2.6.0 gives 63.64 here and breaks 20 of the 22 matched functions, -O3 gives 71.66 and breaks 7 - the preset is right.
Round 441 correction: the giv half of this entry is STALE for that 71.49% body - its column latch has no `addiu sN,sN,124`; it recomputes `sll x,5 / subu / sll 2` per iteration exactly as retail does. The single remaining difference is that the compile keeps `radius` in `$fp` and retail keeps it in stack slot 0x48, which shifts all eleven state slots 4 bytes down and renames every register (267 of 275 lines differ at 71.49% / 1104 / 276). Retail's inner loop wants ten callee-saved registers (col, colCoord, pSprite, poly offset, poly counter, the `colCoord & 0x3F` mask and its +0x10, `rowCoord+0x10`, pPoly) against the ten it has, so `radius` - three refs, lowest allocno priority - is the value that loses. Measured dead ends on that body: three permutations of the 16 s32 declarations (71.49 twice, one with no code difference at all); an explicit `s32 rowNext = rowCoord + 0x10` local for the two uses retail keeps in `$s4` (72.39% / 1096 / 274, frame 0x9C - four bytes short, `radius` still in `$fp`); `volatile s32 radius` (70.63% / 1088 / 272 - it does go to a slot, and the body loses 12 bytes elsewhere). The next attempt must raise the inner loop's register demand by one without adding a stack slot.
- id: g-qimode-index-blocks-the-mult-giv | keywords: loop, giv, strength, reduce, multiply, sll, qimode, u-char, andi
  **A LOOP INDEX THAT `loop.c` TURNS INTO ITS OWN MULT GIV (`move sN,zero` in the preheader, `addiu sN,sN,4` in the latch) KEEPS ITS `sll` ONLY WHEN ITS OPERAND IS A QImode PSEUDO - and the byte counter that does it costs two `andi 0xff`** (round 405, battle/main/misc17 func_80092298, parked 96.97% at retail's exact 1260 bytes / 315 insns under XenoBattle260). Retail's third loop reads three tables off one `s32` counter: `addu at,at,s0` + `lbu %lo(D_800C33B4)(at)`, a hoisted `addiu s2,s2,4` pointer for `D_800C33C4`, and `sll v0,s0,2` + `lh %lo(D_800C3404)(v0)` recomputed every iteration; `D_800C3404[i][0]` with an `s16 [][2]` extern instead emits the giv (one extra `move` in the preheader, 1264 bytes, 96.09%), and `((s16 *)D_800C3404)[i * 2]` or a flat `s16 D_800C3404[]` + `[i * 2]` hoists the table's BASE into a register (`lui/addiu` in the preheader, `lh 0($sN)`, `addiu sN,sN,4`, 1260/315, 96.28%) - so the two ways to avoid the giv each cost exactly what the giv saved. A `u_char` counter, or a `u_char c; c = i;` copy used as the index, is the only spelling measured that reproduces retail's bare `sll` + `%hi/%lo` form (confirmed with cc1_probe.sh on a 20-line snippet, so it is the front end and not the surrounding function), but gcc 2.6.0 does not use the loop's `< 16` range to drop the conversion: it emits `andi $r,$r,0xff` for the index and `andi`+`sltiu` for the test, 317 insns / 1268 bytes / 95.47%. Rule: judge a park that needs the multiply UNREDUCED by whether retail also lacks an `andi` on the index and an `sltiu` test - if it lacks both, the original was not a narrow counter and this is not source-reachable; the table-base-hoist levers (g-strength-reduce-table-base, k-array-base-pointer-local) point the other way on this shape.
  Round 430 addendum (same function, re-measured at 96.97% / retail's 1260 bytes / 315 insns): the mult giv is not dodged by an explicit shift either - `*(s16 *)((u8 *)D_800C3404 + (i << 2))` compiles byte-identically to `D_800C3404[i][0]` (the front end canonicalises the shift back into the multiply, 315/1260, 96.97), and a `s16 (*pColumns)[2]` local for the first table plus `column`/`height` locals reproduces retail's pointer and its `addiu s2,s2,4` but the giv then takes `$s0` and pushes `i` to `$s1` and the cursor to `$s3` (315/1260, 96.97), while a flat `s16 *pColumns` + `pColumns[i * 2]` is 316/1264 / 96.03. Retail's TWO givs (a base-combined pointer for D_800C33C4 plus the unreduced multiply for D_800C3404) are one `mult(i,4)` tree to cse whatever the spelling, so loop.c only ever sees one candidate: check, as here, whether the second table's multiply could not be shared before spending a lane on spellings.
  Round 443 addendum (same function, fourth measurement, 96.97% / 1260 / 315): the "a lone use is not worth reducing" theory is DEAD - with `s16 (*pColumns)[2]` + `pColumns++` for the first table, so that `i * 4` has exactly ONE remaining use (the heights argument), loop.c still emits the mult giv (`move sN,zero` in the preheader, `addiu sN,sN,4` in the latch) and it is that giv, not `i`, that gets `$s0` (i to `$s1`, the cursor to `$s3`, 316 insns / 1264 bytes / 96.09%). So a MULT giv by 4 is reduced even at one use, and retail's `sll v0,s0,2` cannot be explained by profitability: in retail it is not a giv at all. The register order corroborates that retail's only derived giv is the `&D_800C33C4` pointer and it is allocated LAST (i->$s0, cursor->$s1, pointer->$s2) - the mult giv, when loop.c makes one, takes the FIRST slot because its pseudo lives across the whole loop. Do not spend another lane on spellings here until something makes loop.c decline the mult giv outright.
- id: g-stream-counts-are-reread-from | keywords: loop, reload, cse, alias, counter, memory, hoist, invariant
  **A HALFWORD COUNTER THAT RETAIL RELOADS FROM MEMORY AT EVERY USE IS THE SOURCE READING THE POINTER, NOT A LOCAL: the pointer is a `u16 *` into the same block the body stores through, so cse cannot prove it is loop-invariant and a named local is the wrong spelling** (round 403, battle/main/misc23 func_800A7064, parked 75.69% at 2172 bytes against retail's 2276). Retail's point loop ends `lhu v0,0(t1) / nop / slt v0,s0,v0 / bnez` (the bound re-read per iteration, no `andi`, because a `u16` load promotes to int) and the segment loop re-reads the same word five times across 0x1584/0x1614/0x16F0/0x1704/0x17D0. `u16 nCount = *pCount++; for (j = 0; j < nCount; j++)` puts it in a register, adds an `andi 0xffff` for the compare and drops the reloads; writing `for (j = 0; j < *pCount; j++)`, `pSeg->unk2 = nRow + *pCount + 1` and `uRow += pCount[0] + 1` reproduces every one of them (75.49 -> 75.65). Check whether the loads sit after a store through another pointer before assuming a local will do: that is the whole decision.
- id: a-loop-hoists-a-symbolic-table-base-but-straight-line-code-does-not | keywords: hoist, la, at, symbolic, table, loop, giv, probe
  **`arr[idx]` ON AN EXTERN SYMBOL EMITS RETAIL'S `lw %lo(sym)($reg)` (which maspsx turns into `lui $at / addu $at,$at,$reg / lw %lo($at)`) IN STRAIGHT-LINE CODE BUT `la $sN,sym` IN THE PREHEADER PLUS `addu $reg,$reg,$sN / lw 0($reg)` INSIDE A LOOP, so a park whose ONLY residual is that hoisted base is a loop.c giv reduction and not a spelling of the load** (round 432, battle/main/misc16_r2 func_8008FE18, parked 96.18% at retail's exact 1060 bytes / 265 insns, gcc-2.6.0 -O2 -G8). One `cc1_probe.sh` settles it in 15 s: `load(r, g_Digi[i])` straight-line emits `lw $5,g_Digi($2)` (retail's), while `for (i<32) { tens = pu[i]/10; if (tens) load(r, g_Digi[tens]); else load(r, g_Ws); }` emits `la $20,g_Digi` + `addu $2,$2,$20 / lw $5,0($2)` - identical with a `u8` or an `s32` index and with the array declared `[10]` or `[]`, so it is neither the index type nor a known array bound. Retail's object has the `$at` form at all four of that function's digit-buffer sites, so retail's build did not reduce the base; the same unit's OTHER loop (`lbu %lo(g_BattleCommandGridIds)(at)` with a biv index) is not reduced either, i.e. the decision is per loop and the only measured knob that removes a reduction is register pressure (a-g-strength-reduce-table-base) - which retail's loop has none of to spare (it spends s0..s4 on bufs/i/0xcccd/0xd/6 and this build spends a 6th, s5, on the hoisted base). Do not chase index spellings on this shape; probe the loop shape first and park on the probe.
  Round 445 addendum (same function, redo lane, re-measured 96.18 / 1060 bytes / 265 insns under gcc-2.6.0): the two spellings the park listed as untried are dead - `*(bufs + i) = pBuf` for a base-first `addu s0,v0,v1` (identical 96.18, and note this is NOT the misc11_b embedded-assignment case) and the volatile-ELEMENT form `extern u_long * volatile T[]` (identical 96.18; the park's earlier 'volatile on the declaration' was the pointer-volatile reading). Both leave the same residual, so neither the addu row nor `MEM_VOLATILE_P` reaches loop.c's decision. Useful arithmetic for judging a park on this shape: at this loop the hoist trades 4 one-instruction savings (one per site) against 2 insns of `la` plus a 2-insn save/restore, so the total INSTRUCTION COUNT is retail's exactly and only the callee-saved set differs - do not read 'same insn count' as 'the body is a spelling away'.
- id: g-strength-reduce-table-base | keywords: loop, hoist, strength-reduce, giv, table, base, callee-saved, frame
  **A LOOP-VARIANT `table + idx * stride` READ IS TURNED INTO A GIV WHOSE HOISTED BASE EATS A SPARE CALLEE-SAVED REGISTER ONLY WHEN THE BODY LEAVES ONE FREE - PORTING THE TWIN'S EXTRA LIVE VALUES REMOVES IT (round 402, battle/main/misc7 func_8007BD5C, 91.41 -> 99.88 at retail's exact 332 bytes / 83 insns, then 100.00 once the bank's own symbol was named; the park had blamed this on the compiler and had been standing since round 388).** Retail reads the flag byte with the symbol inside the mem operand (`lui $at,%hi; addu $at,$at,v0; lbu %lo($at)`), and the parked body instead hoisted the symbol's address into `$s5` with a 56-byte frame against retail's 48. The unit's misc8 twin is 81 of 83 words identical and does NOT hoist, because its loop body carries two extra live values that retail's asm also has and the park had folded away: a PRELOADED byte read (`u8 tried = buf[idx];` before the call, instead of testing `buf[idx]` after it) and a SECOND re-formed pointer to the same bank for the store path (`u16 *pLoopField = <bank base>;` inside the loop). Both are visible in the .s (`lbu v0,0(s2+s0)` before the `jal`, and `lw/lbu/sll/addu` re-forming the row pointer after it), so when a stub has a twin, port the twin's live-value set before believing a note that says the allocator hoists and cannot be stopped. The last 0.12% was separate: the typed `g_BattleSlotRegs[index].wordRegs` spelling folds the bank's `+0x20` into the relocation and names `g_BattleSlotRegs`, so the untyped byte base `(u16 *)(g_BattleSlotWordRegs + index * 0x40)` is the only spelling that reproduces retail's `g_BattleSlotWordRegs` HI16/LO16 pair.
- id: g-hoisted-loop-invariant-constant | keywords: loop, hoist, cc1, move, prologue
  **A hoisted loop-invariant constant CAN be stopped from source (round 327; this corrects the round-316 note below).** `loop.c` hoists a constant iff `threshold * savings * m->lifetime >= insn_count`, where `threshold = (loop_has_call ? 1 : 2) * (1 + n_non_fixed_regs)`, `savings` is the number of loads of that value (doubled by `combine_movables` when two loads set the same constant), `lifetime` is the luid span between that value's first and last USE, and `insn_count` is the loop's real-insn count. So you shrink `lifetime` by moving the two uses CLOSER together, or raise `savings` by forcing one load per use. Measured on the animation-script VM: `-9` hoisted at life 14-16 and at 7 but NOT at 5-6, and `0xF03FFFFF` hoisted at 7 but not at 6 - eliminating both took the body 72.61 -> 91.20 with its prologue byte-identical to retail's. Diagnose with `cc1 -dL` on the unit's `.i` inside the container and read `Insn N: regno R (life L), move-insn savings S moved / not desirable`. A hoisted TABLE BASE is still not defeatable (the round-316 probes); an invariant CONSTANT is.
  Round 446 addendum (slus_006.64/system/sound SoundSpuMemoryAllocateBlock, re-measured 94.58% / retail's exact 256 bytes / 64 insns, 40 differing lines, all one cause): read the two loop heads side by side to see WHICH shape of the hoist you are fighting. Retail's is a fresh two-instruction constant load (`lui v1,%hi(Arr)` / `addiu v1,v1,%lo(Arr)` in the `lh`'s load-delay slot) because no register in its loop equals `&Arr` (its `la s1` IS pPrevBlock, overwritten by `move s1,a0`); the compile's is a one-instruction `move a2,s0` plus a `nop` filler, because the preheader `la` left `&Arr` in the register that later becomes pPrevBlock, so `move_movables` moved the REG_EQUAL register instead of the symbol. Both are per-iteration copies at the same slot, so this is not 'hoisted vs not hoisted' and the threshold arithmetic above does not apply - and the register snapshot is what then swaps the whole callee-saved pair. Pointer arithmetic on the struct type (`Arr + pPrevBlock->nextBlockIndex`) is byte-identical to the `(u8 *)Arr + (i << 4)` cast form (the 15th spelling on that function).
  Addendum (round 435, battle/main/misc35 func_800C11CC, parked 67.82% / 3700 bytes against retail's 3708): the dump names the numbers on ONE line each, so read it before hunting a spelling - `Insn 935: regno 399 (life 9), move-insn savings 9  moved to 2336` against `Loop from 10 to 2321: 995 real insns` was the ONLY movable in a 927-insn function to move, and it stays 9/9 after two different source attacks. Two facts this measures: **each merged `set reg,const K` load contributes `savings 3`, not 1** (three merged const-1 loads -> savings 9), and **dropping one *use* does not drop the group's savings**: rewriting that function's `(flags & 3) != 1` test as `(flags & 3) - 1 != 0` (which removes the const-1 load entirely) produced byte-identical 925 insns / 3700 bytes with the hoist intact, and hoisting its `1 << mode` into a local whose result is consumed on the next line left both savings AND `life` at 9. So the source-side knob on a wide gap (`81 * threshold >= 995` here) is only reachable when a spelling changes the *mode* of the constant (`a-constant-cse-is-per-mode`) or deletes whole arms; on a body whose merged uses live in arms retail materialises separately, park and record the -dL line instead of permuting.
- id: g-rotated-loop-s-exit | keywords: loop, hoist, addiu, delay, pointer
  **A ROTATED loop's exit compensation is the GUARD-PLUS-BOTTOM-TEST shape** (round 318): `i = 1; if (i < n) { do { ... } while (i < n); }` emits retail's trailing `addiu a0,a0,-0x7C` (the back-edge delay slot executing on exit) and matched at 100.00 where the `for` form is 74.52. If retail's loop ends with an odd subtract or add on the induction pointer, that is the shape to try.
- id: g-do-while-top-copy | keywords: loop, hoist, addiu, lui, move
  **A `do {} while (0);` at the TOP of a copy loop moves the preheader's `move` before the `lui/addiu` symbol** (round 318, `battle/misc4`: the last residual was the loop preheader emitting `lui/addiu a2` before `move a1,v1`). Same block as the store-group lever, different position in the code.
- id: g-two-sequential-do-while | keywords: loop, hoist, li, lui, volatile
  **TWO sequential `do {} while (0)` blocks place hoisted constants where ONE block cannot** (round 325): a do-block's preheader is where `loop.c` hoists one invariant constant per free register, so a single block puts two constants together at its head, while splitting the group into two blocks puts each constant where retail has it - and a constant that must hoist above two volatile stores has to be INSIDE the second block. If a park's residual is two `li`/`lui` constants landing in the wrong places, count the blocks before the registers.
- id: g-hoisted-constant-sits-after | keywords: loop, hoist, li, preheader, argument, la, permuter
  **A hoisted invariant constant that sits AFTER your preheader statements is a preheader SOURCE STATEMENT boundary, and removing the statements to move it costs the copy they were buying.** round 393, movie/main/misc1 func_800704E8 (gcc 2.6.0, parked 99.04 at retail's exact 2276 bytes / 569 insns): retail's preheader is `ori s3,zero,1` / `lui+addiu s4,&g_MovieStreamReadCount` / `addu s2,s1,pWork`, our body emits the last two first and the hoisted `li` last, and the residual is that one row plus the `la a0` of the block's format string. Measured negatives: an empty `do { } while (0);` between the call and the two statements moves nothing (identical rows); inlining the counters base (`pCounter = (u32*)g_MovieStreamReadCount`) drops the s4 copy (97.69, 2264 bytes, 566 insns); assigning `pBase = pWork;` at the top of the in-loop teardown instead of before the loop drops the s2 copy (99.05 but 2272 bytes / 568 insns, so it can never link). Both preheader copies are the source statements, so the hoist's position is not source-reachable from there. A 4-minute permuter run on such a body is blind: base 460 = best 460 over 1067 iterations, because the reloc-name-only rows dominate the score - do not spend a second run on it.
- id: g-switch-dispatch-makes-loop | keywords: loop, hoist, frame, goto, switch, callee-saved, case, lui
  **A `for (;;) { switch ... }` dispatch makes `loop.c` hoist invariants to the FUNCTION preheader; write the dispatch as a `goto` loop instead** (round 329, the strongest lever of that round). `for (;;) { switch (x) { case ...: } }` gives the outer construct LOOP notes, so every invariant constant in every arm is hoisted once, into the function preheader - which is what pushes constants into the wrong callee-saved registers and changes the frame. Rewriting the dispatch as `dispatch: ... goto dispatch;` removes those outer loop notes so EACH inner loop hoists its own copy, which is what retail's per-preheader `lui`/`ori` look like. That closed `field/misc5` func_800A5924's last two residuals with no register change at all (frame 0x40 -> 0x38).
- id: g-while-if-x-k | keywords: loop, hoist, li, move
  **`while (1) { if (x != K) break; ... }` stops `duplicate_loop_exit_test` rotating a loop** where `while (x == K)` rotates it (95.13 vs the matched form), and a **comma init in a `for`** (`for (i = 0, n = 0x2c0; ...)`) is what puts the zero move before the constant's `li`. Both measured on the same body (round 329).
- id: g-rotated-entry-j-to-bottom-test | keywords: loop, hoist, rotated, entry, j, beq, thread, preheader, jtbl
  **A retail loop whose entry evaluates the condition in the PREHEADER and `j`s straight to the single bottom test (`j .Ltest` landing on the `bne`, constants in the two delay slots: `ori a3` in the `j`'s, the comparand's `li` hoisted as far as an earlier branch's delay) was NOT reproduced from ANY loop spelling under gcc 2.6.0, 2.7.2 or 2.7.2-cdk** (round 398, battle/main/misc1 func_800723E0, parked 77.55 at 352 bytes vs retail's 332 / 88 vs 83 insns; probed with cc1_probe.sh under all three compilers). `duplicate_loop_exit_test` gives the two-eval ROTATED form but keeps the entry's own inline branch: while/for/do-while/guarded-do-while/goto-check shapes all emit [preheader: eval; `beq` -> exit][body][eval; `bne` -> body], 4+ instructions over retail's [preheader: eval; `j` bottom-test][body][eval; `bne`]. The threading of the entry branch into the bottom one may need a compiler pass none of the three has (gcc 2.8+? not in tools/). Cost of the residual is also register-wide: the shared branch is what lets retail keep the comparand (`t0`=1) and the wrap constant (`a3`=0xB) in registers across the loop and lets the exit handler reuse the loop's slot register. Park on this shape and say ROTATED_ENTRY; worth re-probing if a 2.8.x preset is ever added. Companion structural facts from the same body, all measured: an if/else chain with the reset-call arm LAST gives retail's `bnez`-forward-to-end block layout (`if (mask == 0) { ... } else { Reset(); }`), a `func(0)` call shared by two arms placed AFTER the inner if/else cross-jumps into the one call block retail shares three ways, and a mid-loop `func(0); return;` exit jumps directly to that shared block just like retail's `beq` at the loop head.
- id: g-construct-decides-loop-c | keywords: loop, hoist, do-while, goto
  **THE CONSTRUCT DECIDES `loop.c`'s INVARIANT HOISTING, not only the cost model** (round 338): `field/misc4` func_80078D44 refused to match while its stream loop was written `for`/`while`, and the `do { ... } while (ArchiveDataSync() != 0);` form is what stopped retail's constants being hoisted out of the loop - the body then matched at 100.00 with retail's exact 1348 bytes. So before tuning the cost model (round 327's lifetime/savings rule), change the loop CONSTRUCT: do-while, while, guard+bottom-test and goto each give loop.c different notes.
- id: g-pointer-local-what-makes | keywords: loop, hoist, lbu, frame, lui, pointer, struct
  **A POINTER LOCAL IS WHAT MAKES A LOOP-HOISTED ADDRESS SHARE ONE BASE** (round 336): retail keeps `&A`, `&B`, `&C` in three saved registers across a loop and reaches each by displacement (`lbu 1(s0)` for the byte after it), where writing the globals inline gives `lui $v0,%hi(sym+1); lbu 0(v0)` at every use (87.16, +44 bytes, frame 0x48). Source-level `p = &global;` assignments placed JUST BEFORE the loop reproduce retail's preheader exactly and took battle/misc14 func_80089CCC 87.16 -> 99.01 -> 100.00 (RELOC). The pair `D_800C3E28` + `g_BattleLastMenuEvent` is one 2-byte struct, and typing it that way is what makes the base shareable.
- id: g-induction-base-fix-worth | keywords: loop, hoist, pointer, struct, addu, lui, s32
  THE INDUCTION-BASE FIX (worth three functions in one unit): when a loop stub is stuck at 99.3-99.6% with only base-register/offset differences, write the loop as an INDEXED access ('pSlot[i].field' plus an s32 i) instead of an incrementing pointer ('p->field; p++'). With p++ gcc's combine_givs re-bases the induction register onto the address of the body's LAST memory reference, so every displacement is shifted by the struct offset; with p[i] the biv is p + i*stride, the base register stays the symbol, and the displacements are the literal struct offsets - retail's shape. Try this before any other source shape. Counter-example: when the indexed form makes gcc reach every field through a lui $at / addu $at,$at,$s0 sequence (a folded HI16 addend) it has gone too far and the pointer form was better (field/main/misc4 func_8007A5C4, 77.28% vs 73.51%).
- id: g-second-loop-invariant-lever | keywords: loop, hoist, pointer, move, permuter
  THE SECOND-LOOP-INVARIANT LEVER (round 170, battle/misc8 func_8007C9D4, 98.55 -> 100): when retail's loop preheader ends with a COPY of a loop-invariant pointer ('move sX,a1') and yours coalesces it away, the source had a SECOND variable holding the same pointer value, re-derived AFTER a loop-body use of another loop-invariant. gcc's move_movables hoists both into the preheader in body order, which reproduces retail's order, and the copy survives because its source pseudo dies in the previous block (func_8007D478 in the same file closed with the identical shape; a 4-minute permuter run on the 98.43% body found nothing).
- id: g-do-while-block-also | keywords: loop, hoist, pointer
  THE DO/WHILE BLOCK ALSO FORCES A LOAD ORDER, and it may be EMPTY: an empty 'do { } while (0);' after a pointer local stopped gcc 2.7.2 hoisting a global load above an address formation in world_map/misc7's func_80081174 (100.00% with it, 65.26% without). Leave a one-line comment - it looks gratuitous and is not.
- id: g-retail-s-loop-shape | keywords: loop, hoist, cse, delay
  Retail's loop shape is while (1) { if (pEntry->unk0 == 0xFFFF) break; pEntry++; i++; }, not while (cond): when the original has a top test, a j back edge and both increments in the delay slots, gcc inverts a while (cond) into tests at both ends with bne (field/main/misc8 func_80085788: 72.15% -> byte-identical inner scan). Same function: a global read at three sites is RELOADED by gcc each time (it does not CSE a global across calls), so one local is required.
- id: g-loop-bound-spelling-x | keywords: loop, hoist, slti, li, addiu, switch
  Loop bound spelling: `for (x = 0; x != N; x++)` gives retail's `li v0,N; bne`; `x < N` gives `slti/bnez` instead (round 44, battle/misc28: worth 6 instructions). If your C is right and the loop tail differs by exactly that, switch the comparison. gcc 2.6.0 is the opposite: in a unit on that compiler it rewrites an ascending `slti/bnez` count loop into a countdown (`li a0,N-1 / addiu -1 / bgez`), and the only source lever found to keep retail's `slti` is a zero-statement `do { } while (0);` at the end of the body (round 56, menu/misc9) - report that ROUGH rather than shipping the empty block.
- id: g-unrotated-top-tested-loop | keywords: loop, hoist, li, s32
  Unrotated top-tested loop with `li t2,-1` hoisted ONCE before it: write `s32 limit = -1; while (1) { if (--count == limit) break; ... }`. The `while (1)`/break form stops gcc's duplicate_loop_exit_test rotation, and the LIMIT LOCAL (not a literal -1, not the parameter) is what hoists the li (round 47, system/temp2 func_80026F44: 72.66 -> 74.63% at retail's exact size).
- id: g-read-bump-lever-round | keywords: loop, hoist, addiu, delay
  THE READ-AND-BUMP LEVER (round 121, four functions in menu/misc7 from one find): when retail has the counter's 'addiu' in the BRANCH DELAY SLOT of the loop condition's own test and no latch copy, the source both read and bumped the index in ONE expression and kept the running value in a local - 'for (i = 0; i < 9; ) { digit = g_Menu->digits[i++]; ... }' - and a second running variable carried the x argument, incremented at the END of the body ('for (i = 0, x = a0; i < 9; ) { ...; x += 8; }'), which is what puts 'addiu s1,s1,8' in the back-branch slot. 'digits[i]' twice plus 'for (...; i++)' can never produce that shape; a for-loop latch increment cannot either.

- id: g-while-entered-with-no-top-test | keywords: loop, do-while, while, rotated, entry, top-test, poll, duplicate-exit
  **A LOOP RETAIL ENTERS WITH NO TOP TEST AND LEAVES WITH A BACKWARD BRANCH WAS A `do {} while` IN THE SOURCE** (round 400, field/main/misc9 func_800ABA98, 82.61 -> 84.86 = 2.37 points, the whole control flow then matching). Retail falls straight from the previous loop's exit into `jal FieldClearAndSwapOTag / ... / andi v0,v0,0x100 / beqz v0,body`: no entry test at all. Written as `while (!(g_FieldButtonsPressedOnce & 0x100)) { ... }` cc1 keeps a top-test block and puts the test at the bottom, which costs an extra `j` in the PREVIOUS loop's exit (the exit must jump forward over the body to the test). `do {...} while (!(...));` reproduces retail instruction for instruction. So read the loop's ENTRY path, not only its latch: a `while` has an entry test, a do-while does not, and the previous loop's exit shape is what shows which one the original was.
- id: g-giv-per-loop-level-is-not-source-reachable | keywords: loop, giv, strength, duplication, spill, frame, park, biv, spill-count
  **`loop.c` GIVES THE SAME TABLE-FIELD REFERENCE ITS OWN GIV AT EACH NESTING LEVEL, AND WHICH REFS GET ONE IS NOT SOURCE-REACHABLE - it sets the frame, the spills and the instruction count of any loop reading a table's fields** (round 400, field/main/misc9 func_800AB808, parked 68.25% at 592 bytes vs retail's 668 / 148 vs 164 insns). Retail reads the four fields of `RECT D_800AF5C0[5]` through SIX givs - three at the outer level (y, w, h) plus three fresh copies inside the inner `memcpy` loop - and re-forms `x` from the symbol every inner iteration, so the loop counter (0x38) and the rect walker (0x48) spill, pFile/pImage spill to 0x28/0x30, and the frame is 0x80. The same C gives three givs (x, y, h), keeps the counter in a register and re-forms w instead (frame 0x58, 148 insns). Measured dead on it: the table inline (`D_800AF5C0[i].x` everywhere, so gcc reaches each field through `lui $at / addu $at,$at,$s18`), through a local pointer, and as word arithmetic `(u_long*)tim.paddr + (j + y) * 0x50 + x / 4` - the last one IS what the original object wrote (its relocation names D_800AF5C6, i.e. the `lh 0(D_800AF5C0+6)(reg)` macro form) and it still emits the same 148 instructions. Park on a giv-count difference; `twin_for` and m2c have nothing to port, because the difference is inside strength_reduce.
- id: g-derived-giv-only-when-the-biv-init-is-outside | keywords: giv, induction, loop, biv, init, preheader, duplicate, s-register
  **`loop.c` DERIVES A `biv + const` GIV ONLY WHEN THE BIV'S INITIALISER SITS OUTSIDE THE LOOP, SO A PARK WHOSE ENTIRE RESIDUAL IS THAT ONE DERIVED REGISTER HAS NO SOURCE SPELLING: the giv-less shape needs the init inside the body, which re-runs it at the loop header (an infinite loop) and duplicates it into a preceding branch's delay slot** (round 443, movie/main/misc2 func_80075D8C, 308 insns, XenoMovie260G0 = gcc-2.6.0 -O2 -G0; the honest body is 96.43% / 1248 bytes / 312 insns against retail's 1232 / 308 and the init-inside body is 99.66% / 1236 with one duplicate `move s0,s1`). The loop is `j = entry; do { ... ArchiveDecodeSector(j + 1) ... j++; } while (j < entry + 0x14);`; retail recomputes `addiu a0,s0,1` at all three `j+1` call sites and saves s0-s6, ours keeps `s1 = j + 1` (`addiu s1,s0,1` twice at the loop head, `move a0,s1` at the sites, `addiu s1,s1,1` before the latch's `addiu s0,s0,1`) and saves s0-s7 with a frame 8 bytes bigger. Nothing else in the 308 instructions moves, so read the two rows together: a duplicated preheader copy PLUS a latch `+1` on a register retail does not have is this wall, not two bugs. Measured dead on the same body: the `for (j = entry; j < entry + 0x14; j++)` form 95.49% / 1260 / 315, a `next = j + 1` local 96.49, `++j` in the condition and `1 + j` operand order 96.43, gcc-2.7.2 95.11 (so the 2.6.0 preset stands), and two permuter runs (6 min from the init-inside body base 112 best 112, one from the init-outside body base 1481). `j + 1` cannot be made non-`biv + const` while still emitting `addiu` (an invariant variable addend buys `addu` and a register retail does not use), which is what closes it.
  Companion rule for the same shape: when the duplicate lands in a delay slot of a test that ENDS the preceding statement group, the trailing `+1`/`move` in that slot is `reorg` filling from the loop head, not a second source assignment - do not spend runs reordering the statements around it.

- id: g-invariant-product-hoist | keywords: loop, hoist, invariant, mult, mflo, cse, reload, array
  **A THREE-BYTE LOCAL STORED PER ITERATION IS WHAT FORCES RETAIL'S RE-READS OF A SOURCE POINTER, and the invariant products beside them are still hoisted whatever the spelling** (round 427, world_map/main/misc13 func_800931D8, parked 65.59% at 356 bytes / 87 insns against retail 380 / 92). The inner body reads `*pIn` FOUR times in retail (`lhu v0,0(a3)` after each of three `sb` stores) because the source stores the extracted components into a `u8 rgb[3]` local and then writes the mix expression with `*pIn` again: gcc 2.7.2 has no alias analysis, so each byte store to the stack kills the CSEd load, while the `rgb[k]` reads themselves come back from the register cse recorded for the store (there is NO `lbu` from the stack). Writing the four reads as a single `v = *pIn` local gives one load and a 316-byte body; the `u8 rgb[3]` spelling reproduces the four loads, the three `sb`s in retail's positions, `bnez` with no `andi` (the test must read `*pIn`, not a u16 local) and `srl ...15` (so the row ratio and its complement are `u32`; `s32` emits `sra`). What it does NOT reproduce: loop.c hoists the three invariant products `fr*t`, `fg*t`, `fb*t` from the pixel loop into the row loop (a mult+mflo pair each = exactly the 24 missing bytes, and 3 saved registers instead of retail's 5) and every measured spelling hoists them - three statements into a `u32` accumulator (byte-identical), `t*fr` operand order, `u8*` vs `u16*` parameters, `s32`/`u8` factor locals. `pFactors[k]` inline in the loop keeps the products in place (65.81, 368 bytes, 90 insns) but adds three `lbu`. Preset screened and not the answer: gcc-2.6.0 58.52, gcc-2.7.2-cdk does not compile the body.
- id: g-invariant-addend-keeps-mul | keywords: loop, hoist, pointer, biv, sll, addu, multiply, strength
  **AN INVARIANT ADDITIVE TERM IN THE INDEX EXPRESSION IS WHAT STOPS strength reduction of `base + index * stride` into a pointer biv** (round 389, battle/main/misc1 func_800728B8: park 78.84% -> 100.00, retail's exact 128 bytes). When the base is a loop-invariant PARAMETER, `for (...) { AddPrim(ot, &pBase[index]); index += 2; }` makes loop.c's combine_givs fold the base into a pointer induction variable (`addiu p,p,0x50` per iteration, one extra callee-saved register, frame 0x28 -> 0x30). Retail instead recomputes the product every iteration (`sll a1,s0,2` in the back-edge delay slot, then `addu a1,a1,s0`, `sll a1,3` = synth_mult's `(x*5)<<3` for 40) and adds the base with `addu a1,s3,a1` - which is what you get by folding the step into the index and keeping the start as an ADDEND: `&pBase[i * 2 + primStart]` with `i` the plain `for` counter. The addend makes the giv a sum of two induction quantities, so only the `i*2 + start` part is reduced (to the `s0 += 2` biv) and the multiply survives. The already-matched twins of this stub, `ShopMenuRenderString` (shop_menu/main/misc) and `MenuRenderString`, are written exactly that way; a body that increments the index in the loop instead is 78% forever.

- id: g-source-local-holding-the-doubled-index | keywords: loop, strength, biv, giv, sll, frame, callee-saved, global
  **WHEN THE INDEX IS `symbol + i * 2` AND THE SYMBOL IS A GLOBAL (a reload per use, so no invariant addend exists), a SOURCE LOCAL assigned `index2 = i * 2;` as the loop body's FIRST STATEMENT is what stops loop.c folding `i*2` into a `+= 2` biv - and that one line buys the frame, the saved set and the in-loop `sll`** (round 429, battle/main/misc1 func_80072938, 89.10 -> 95.62% at 348 bytes against retail's 352, XenoBattle260). With the index written inline (`pPrim[g_BattlePrimStartIndex + i * 2]`, either operand order) this cc1 derives `i` from a doubled biv: `sll s0,a1,1` in the preheader, `addiu s0,s0,2` in the latch, the loop test rewritten as `slt s0,2*last`, five saved registers and a 0x28 frame. Retail keeps `i` raw (`sll s0,s2,1` at the loop top, `addiu s2,s2,1`, `slt v0,s2,s3`), six saved registers and a 0x30 frame, which the `index2` local reproduces exactly - the same first-statement-local trick that the `g-invariant-addend-keeps-mul` body gets for free from a parameter addend, and it is needed BECAUSE the global is reloaded and therefore cannot supply that addend. Residual left in that park (95.62, 4 bytes short): with `i` dead at the top of the body, loop.c emits the latch increment before the last store's address chain, so reorg steals it into the `lw symbol` load-delay slot and the b0 store into the `bnez` slot, where retail has the increment after the store and two `nop`s. Measured dead on the same body: `s32` return type (the `c-stolen-delay-slot-retail` lever), a while-loop with `i++` as the body's last statement, and the index inline (89.10 both operand orders).

- id: g-alias-pointer-cannot-hold-a-base | keywords: loop, hoist, preheader, move, lui, biv, alias, move-order
  **A PLAIN ALIAS POINTER CANNOT PUSH A SYMBOL'S `lui/addiu` AHEAD OF ANOTHER INIT IN THE PREHEADER; only a WALKING pointer keeps the base in the entry block, and that costs a guard** (round 393, battle/main/misc14 func_8008A684, parked 99.03% at retail's exact 828 bytes / 207 insns). Retail's loop preheader is `[t0 = g_BattleStateWork][lui/addiu a2 = D_800D32F8][move a0,zero][addiu t2,a2,12]`; the parked body emits `move a0,zero` BEFORE the a2 base, and since both are loop.c inits (a0 = the byte offset, a2 = the array base) the insertion is newest-biv-first, so retail's a2 must have come from the ENTRY block. Measured: `pSlot = D_800D32F8;` used as `pSlot[i]` is copy-propagated back to the array form (byte-identical output, as is the same init in the for-comma list), so it cannot supply that entry-block instruction; `for (pSlot = D_800D32F8, offset = 0; pSlot < D_800D32F8 + 3; pSlot++)` does keep the base in the entry block but the pointer compare buys a pre-test guard plus `sltu` (94.76%, 852 bytes, 213 insns). Not a preset question: 99.03 under 2.6.0 -O2 and -O3, plain 2.7.2 -O2, and 2.6.0 -G0; cdk 85.29, cdk -G0 71.94. A third permuter run (base=60 best=60, 867 iterations) found nothing.

- id: g-two-source-inits-emit-in-source-order | keywords: loop, preheader, biv, move-order, do-while, pointer-walk, slt, strength
  **TWO SOURCE INITIALISERS THAT BOTH BECOME `loop.c` INITS IN THE PREHEADER ARE EMITTED IN SOURCE ORDER, so a residual showing `move idx,zero` before a symbol's `lui/addiu` is fixed by initialising the WALKING POINTER BEFORE the index - and the pointer walk has to be a `do {} while` with a signed compare, because the index-loop form makes that base a strength-reduced biv instead** (round 418, battle/main/misc14 func_8008A684, 99.03 -> 100.00 at retail's exact 828 bytes / 207 insns, XenoBattle260; this CLOSES the wall claimed by `g-alias-pointer-cannot-hold-a-base`). Retail's preheader is `[la a2 = D_800D32F8][move a0,zero][addiu t2,a2,12]` where a0 walks the 8-byte counter stride and a2 walks the actor-pointer array. The body that reproduces it: `pSlot = D_800D32F8; offset = 0; do { ... (*pSlot)->unk15FA ... ; offset += 8; pSlot++; } while ((int)pSlot < (int)(D_800D32F8 + 3));` - with the pointer assignment written BEFORE `offset = 0` the `la` prints first; swapping those two statements (offset first) is the 99.03 body, so the statement order alone is worth the match. Load-bearing, each measured: (1) the `for (i = 0; i < 3; i++)` index loop with `D_800D32F8[i]`, or a folded alias `pSlot[i]`, is 99.03 whatever the statement order, because the base is then a strength-reduced biv and its init goes before the source biv's; (2) the `for` form of the pointer walk buys a pre-test guard plus `sltu` (94.76, 852 bytes, 213 insns) - `do {} while` has no top test because retail's loop is entered with none; (3) the `(int)` casts make the compare `slt` (retail's) where a plain pointer compare emits `sltu` (99.71); (4) `offset = i * 8` inside the body makes the offset a derived giv, which flips the preheader correctly but moves the loop test onto the offset (`slti a0,24`, 824 bytes), so the offset must stay a real biv. Measured and dead (all 99.03 or worse): `offset`/`i` as `u32` (98.74 and 92.56), `i <= 2`, `*(D_800D32F8 + i)`, `offset = offset + 8`, a `sizeof` stride, declaration reordering, dropping the unused `pSlot`, and moving `offset += 8` earlier in the body (it moves the latch increment but NOT the preheader, which disproves the "newest-biv-first" reading in `g-alias-pointer-cannot-hold-a-base`).
- id: g-biv-init-and-latch-move-together | keywords: loop, hoist, biv, preheader, prologue, callee-saved, pointer, strength
  **RETALL'S BIV INIT POSITION IN THE PREHEADER AND ITS `addiu +4` IN THE LATCH MOVE TOGETHER, so a residual that shows BOTH is one source cause, not two** (round 394, movie/main/misc1 func_80072F98, parked 98.67 at retail's exact 912 bytes / 226 insns). Retail's six pointer inits are `[pColB][pTargetB][pColG][pTargetG][steps-base][pColR][pTargetR]` and its six latch increments are the same order; ours puts the strength-reduced steps base LAST in both places, because `strength_reduce`/`move_movables` insert a hoisted biv after every source statement of the block. Reading retail's order as "the last two pointers are compiler-DERIVED givs (`combine_givs` emits their init AND their increment after the biv's own)" is the only shape that explains both rows at once. Do not chase the two rows separately: `pStep[i]` (932 bytes), `*pStep`+`pStep++` (880), a mid-block `if (i == 0) {...}` (no change) and the typed `g_Table[i].field` form (896 bytes, 222 insns, the derived base chosen as `+2` = retail's) were all measured on it; the typed form is 4 short because the plain load is CSEd where retail re-reads, and the `volatile` cast that buys the re-read kills the biv derivation (frame 0x60, 90.47). Swapping two independent `pX += 4` lines moves objdiff's fuzzy 98.67 -> 98.71 without changing the residual - another non-monotone score. **ROUND 419 CLARIFIED THE SHAPE AND WHY IT IS UNREACHABLE: retail's ordering is produced by the six-derived-giv (typed array) body ONLY, and that body cannot also produce the re-reads.** Measured: the full typed form (`g_MovieDarkColours[i].r/.g/.b` for all six walks, no pointer locals) emits retail's preheader order exactly - colours base at +2, target base at +2, the -1/-1 derived inits, the steps base, then the -2/-2 derived inits, and the same order in the latch - but is 222 insns / 896 bytes / 90.47% because gcc CSEs the three sum reads retail re-reads; making those three reads volatile on that body drops the giv derivation and gives 246 insns / 992 bytes / 81.67%; `pColB[-2]` / `pTargetB[-2]` in place of the two R pointer locals folds the offsets inline as displacements with no derived giv at all (224 insns / 904 bytes / 95.77%); and the hybrid that keeps a source `s32* pStep` walking pointer while durations stay indexed by `i` makes the durations base a hoisted register and shrinks the frame to 0x48 (218 insns / 880 / 93.25%) - the same failure as `*pStep` + `pStep++`. So the six source bivs are the only body that yields the three re-reads, and the source order of their initialisers cannot move the steps biv init out of last place. Treat as a documented park.
- id: g-sentinel-guard-must-deref | keywords: loop, sentinel, constant, register, loop-carried, load, guard, cse
  **A `-1`-TERMINATED POINTER WALK CAN HAVE EITHER RETAIL'S BARE `li` SENTINEL OR RETAIL'S SEPARATE GUARD LOAD, NOT BOTH: the two source shapes trade one residual for the other** (round 398, world_map/main/misc9 func_8008868C, parked 76.81% at 152 bytes vs retail's 148). Retail: guard `lh v1,0(a1); beq v1,a0` with `li a0,-1` in the entry block, then body `lh a0,0(s0) / addiu s0,s0,4 / lh a1,0(s1) / addiu s1,s1,4 / addu / jal`, then bottom test `lh v1,0(s0); li v0,-1; bne v1,v0`, i.e. the sentinel is a bare CONST_INT at the compare AND the guard re-loads the element the next body iteration loads. `if (*pX != -1) { do { s32 vx = *pX; s32 vy = *pY; pX += 2; pY += 2; f(base+vx, base+vy); } while (*pX != -1); }` reproduces every load, both `addu`s and the jal operands (76.81%) but cse registers the twice-used -1 (`li s3,-1` in the preheader, +4 frame bytes), which displaces the bne delay slot's `li v0,1`. The plain `while (*pX != -1) { vx = *pX; ... }` (ONE static use of the sentinel) DOES leave the sentinel a CONST_INT, but loop.c then reuses the guard's halfword load as the next iteration's body value (`lhu $6,0($16)` hoisted, `sll/sra` re-extension in the body, frame 0x38): 69.00%, 168 bytes. An early `if (*pX == -1) return 1;` before the do-while merges back to the identical 76.81 body. So on this shape park unless you can break the constant's cse by MODE (`a-constant-cse-is-per-mode`) - the sentinel and both comparisons are SImode here, so there is no spelling that does.
- id: g-loop-form-sets-the-copied | keywords: loop, rotated, slti, delay, duplication, reorg, increment, while
  **THE LOOP'S SOURCE FORM SETS HOW MANY TIMES REORG COPIES THE EXIT TEST INTO DELAY SLOTS - `do {} while` gives one copy, `while` gives three, retail has two with the increment in the slot after the call** (round 398, battle/main/misc9 func_8007DA1C, 75.77 -> 95.38% by moving the same body from `for (i = 3; i < 0xB; i++)` to `while`; parked at 352 bytes against retail's 348, so neither form can link - one instruction either way). Retail's `slti v0,s1,0xB` appears in the `beqz v1` (mask test) delay slot and again at the join label plus the latch - two copies - and its `beqz v0` (ClearRecordIdle result) delay slot holds `addiu s1,s1,1`, i.e. retail incremented `i` unconditionally BETWEEN the call and the test. With `idx = i++` cc1 puts the increment at the loop head, so that slot takes a COPY of the join's `slti` instead and the body is one instruction too long; the do-while rotation leaves ONE `slti` and is one short. Getting the increment into that slot needs the call result in a local, which wrecks the allocation (s32 idle 88.25%, 336 bytes; increment at the body end 88.67%, 332), so this family's residual is the increment POSITION, not the loop form - but measure the while form first on the sibling stubs (DB78/DCF8/DE78/DFD4), since it also carries the func_8007D8C0 R370 two-pointer recipe.

- id: a-loop-counter-set-before-the | keywords: loop, preheader, li, counter, for, allocation, order
  **A `li` FOR A LOOP COUNTER LANDS IN FRONT OF THE POINTER SETUP WHEN THE COUNTER IS ITS OWN STATEMENT ABOVE THE LOOP, NOT IN THE `for` INITIALISER** (round 400, world_map/misc16 func_80099708, 91.34 -> 94.82 at retail's exact 260 bytes / 65 insns). Retail's preheader is `lui t0,0x1f80 / move t3,s0 / li t7,8 / lui t9,%hi(table) / lui t6,%hi(col) / lw t6 / li t8,-1`; with `for (oi = 8; oi != -1; oi--)` the `li t7,8` is emitted with the rest of the preheader after the table's `lui/addiu`, and with `oi = 8;` as a statement above a `for (; oi != -1; oi--)` it comes out in retail's position. The loop body, the -1 sentinel and the back-branch delay slots are identical either way, so this is worth one edit on any preheader whose only residual is the position of one `li`.
- id: g-giv-anchor-follows-source-order | keywords: giv, base, anchor, combine, displacement, store, order, reduce
  **THE REDUCED-GIV ANCHOR IS THE LAST DEST-ADDRESS GIV `loop.c` RECORDS, RECORDING FOLLOWS *SOURCE* ORDER, AND A LATE-COMPUTED STORE GROUP CAN STILL SIT LAST IN THE ASM - so when retail's base is `addiu s0,s2,0x25` and yours is `0x20`, move the store GROUP whose anchor you want to be LAST UP IN THE SOURCE, not down.** (round 404, world_map/main/misc7 func_80081D80, 99.88 -> 100.00 at retail's exact 564 bytes / 141 insns, a park standing since round 377.) Retail keeps the quad pointer at `prim+0x25` (the last byte field, `v3`) and addresses all sixteen corner stores at 0..-0x1D; the parked body picked `prim+0x20` (the last `sh`, `x3`) with every displacement 5 larger, same addresses.
