Three probes pin the rule: a duplicate `pQuad->v3 = i + 1;` appended after the x stores moved the base to 37 (+1 insn, 99.29% / 568 bytes, the duplicate emitted next to the FIRST v3 store), moving `x0` last moved it to 8, and the fix was to write the four `x` stores ABOVE the `y2/y3/v2/v3` group: their values come from the `rand() % *pFade - *pFade / 2` delta, so cc1 sinks them below it in the emitted code and the ASM store order stays retail's (`y2,y3,v2,v3` then `x0..x3`) while `v3` is the last giv recorded. Do not trust the asm store order as source order when a store group's values are computed late in the body; the park note's "source statement order cannot reproduce it" was wrong for that reason. The indexed form (`pQuad[i].field`) is not the answer here: 79.45% / 139 insns.


- id: g-s16-counter-pre-decrement | keywords: loop, counter, s16, sll, decrement, tail, narrow, move
  **A COUNTDOWN TAIL OF `addiu v0,a2,-1 / move a2,v0 / sll v0,v0,0x10 / bnez v0` IS A `s16` COUNTER WITH `--count`; a two-variable tail lets gcc test the loop-carried copy instead and emits `sll v0,a2,0x10`** (round 404, slus_006.64/system/sound func_8003C4C4, 99.92 -> 99.96). The `move` into the loop-carried register plus a `sll 16` on the fresher value is the HImode pre-decrement: `s16 count = elementCount; do { ... } while (--count != 0);` reproduced it exactly, where the parked `next = count - 1; count = next;` tested through `(s16)next` was one instruction off (gcc knows the two are equal and sign-extends the carried one). Measured dead on the same body: `s32 count` with `--count` drops the `sll` (a word test needs no extension), `u16 count` with `--count` folds the decrement into `addu a2,a2,t3` off a hoisted 0xFFFF plus `andi 0xffff` (98.42, one instruction SHORT), and a post-decrement tests the OLD value so it never gives the `move`-then-`sll` pair. Check the counter's declared width and the operator position before changing the loop's source form. **SAME LEVER, THE PRE-INCREMENT SIDE (round 420, slus_006.64/system/archive func_80029EB0, 93.07 -> 94.47%, 960 -> 948 bytes): a PCclose retry tail retail emits as `beqz a0,done / jal GfxDrawArchiveReadRetry / addiu v0,s0,1 / sll v0,v0,0x10 / sra v1,v0,0x10 / slti v0,v1,4 / bnez v0,loop / bnez v1,ret-6` narrows ONCE and reuses $v1 for both the loop test and the trailing `if (nClose != 0)`; `while ((nClose = f())) { g(nClose); nClose = nClose + 1; if (nClose >= 4) break; }` keeps the value in the call-result register, narrows twice and costs the two `move $vN` copies plus a `sll` (3 instructions). The body that reproduces it is a do/while with the counter incremented IN THE CONDITION: `do { nClose = f(); if (nClose == 0) break; g(nClose); } while (++nClose < 4);` then `if (nClose != 0) return -6;` - the break path jumps straight past the -6 test, which is exactly retail's `beqz` target. So the same rule holds for both directions: write the counter as the loop variable and put the increment inside the loop condition, not as a separate statement, whenever retail's tail tests with the freshly narrowed register.

- id: g-at-symbol-plus-register-is-assembler-only | keywords: at, addu, lui, symbol, register, strength, hoist, absolute
  **`lui $at,%hi(SYM); addu $at,$at,$off; lhu %lo(SYM)($at)` IS THE ASSEMBLER'S EXPANSION OF ONE `SYM($off)` OPERAND, SO NO C REPRODUCES IT - and the two C shapes that come closest each fail on the opposite register** (round 411, battle/main/misc1 func_8007171C, parked 78.73% under 2.6.0 at 400 bytes against retail's 416; -G0 78.73, 2.7.2 80.27, 2.7.2-cdk compile-failed). $at is gas/ASPSX's own scratch register, so an address that reaches it can only come from a symbolic operand plus a register, not from anything cc1 allocates. The inline spelling `*(u16 *)((u8 *)SYM + off)` with `off` a for-clause biv scores 70.61 and makes a POINTER BIV per symbol (lhu 0(a1) with `addiu a1,a1,0x170`), even for 2-use expressions like `SYM + i * 2`; re-assigning a pointer local at each site (`pWord = (u16 *)((u8 *)SYM + off); ... *pWord ...`) scores 78.73 and gives no biv, but cc1 hoists the symbol's `lui/addiu` pair into the preheader and every site becomes a single `addu a0,a1,t2`. Same two-way split as the misc1 func_800728B8 lever, so measure it, do not re-spell it: a diff whose per-site address forming is 2 insns in retail and 3 in yours (one instruction and one $at) is a park.
  SUPERSEDED FOR world_map/main/misc9 func_8008615C (round 443, 95.40 -> MATCH at retail's exact 644 bytes / 161 insns, check_bin OK): the `la $21,D_8009D618` was NOT a compiler-version signature, it was a body problem, and the recipe transfers to any diff whose symptom is "one extra callee-saved register and a 0x30 frame instead of 0x28 because gcc splits a symbol address that retail reaches with the `$at` macro". Four edits closed it, in this order of payoff: (1) hoist a loop-invariant global READ out of the call arguments into its own block-local assignment inside the same arm (`otHead = g_WorldMapRenderContext[0x1C];` then pass `otHead`) - that alone dropped the split, the s5 save/restore and the frame back to 0x28, 98.50%; (2) spell the scratchpad copy as `pDst[0x34 + i]` off `pDst = (u16*)0x1F800000` instead of `pDst = (u16*)0x1F800068` with `*pDst++` - retail keeps 0x1F800000 in the register and stores at displacement 0x68, and the +0x68-in-the-immediate form costs a `lui/ori` pair; (3) address the two s16 globals as ONE base with a negative displacement (`extern s16 g_WorldMapGridTileRowOrigin[]; pRowOrigin = g_WorldMapGridTileRowOrigin; ... pRowOrigin[0] ... pRowOrigin[-2]`, retail's disps 0 and -4) - declaring them as two scalars, or as a struct at the lower one, makes gcc form `lui %hi / lh 0` for the second and costs a register; (4) `pEntry = pTable - (-id)` for a struct-index sum whose retail form is IN PLACE on the index (`sll v0,v0,0x3 / addu v0,v0,s3`): the plain `pTable + id`, `&pTable[id]`, `id * 8 + pTable` and the inlined-index form all give the mirrored `addu a0,s3,v0` with the address in a reload scratch, and only the double negation ties it to the dying index. Residual after all four is 99.97%, and it is splat's own hi/lo inference (the copy loop's loop-back `addiu $v1,$v1,2` was paired with the `lui $v1,0x1F80` above it and printed as `%lo(D_1F800002)`, so the expected object has imm 0 plus a reloc and the C has the literal) - report that as MATCH/RELOC with check_bin, not as a compiler miss. Note the two permuter runs on the old body found (1) and (4) here, and both were led by the `-fno-rerun-cse-after-loop`/"2.6.0-only shape" reasoning being wrong: measure a loop-invariant ARGUMENT read as a local before concluding a preset split.
  Round 429 measured the sibling case in world_map/main/misc9 func_8008615C (parked 95.40% / 660 bytes against retail's 640, XenoWorldMap): the split is a COMPILER-VERSION signature, not a source one. The same body under gcc-2.7.2 emits `la $21,D_8009D618` in the loop preheader plus `addu $2,$2,$21` per access (so the base costs a sixth callee-saved register: s5, frame 0x30), while gcc-2.6.0 emits the macro form `lh $3,D_8009D618($2)` - and both are visible in cc1's own `.s`, before maspsx. `-fno-rerun-cse-after-loop` under 2.7.2 keeps every matched function of that unit at 100.00 and still splits, so the post-loop CSE is not the pass involved. Because the unit's other eleven matched functions drop to 55-97 under 2.6.0, this is a mixed-TU artefact to report (PRESET_260), not a switch; two further spellings measured on the way both split: an explicit `slot` biv (`D_8009D618[slot + i]`, 90.80 / 672, splits the tile-id base too, s5 AND s6) and the byte-cast form `*(s16*)((u8*)D_8009D618 + (row * 5 + i) * 2)` (95.40 / 660, identical code).
- id: battle-go-loop-left-justified-himode-iv | keywords: induction, giv, sll, sra, shift, scaled, lui, multiply
  **A SCALED LOOP ARGUMENT RETAIL TRACKS AS `lui 0x2A` + `sra a2,s1,0x10` + `+0x200000` PER ROW IS A `s32` LOCAL KEPT LEFT-JUSTIFIED BY 16, NOT THE SImode EXPRESSION** (round 411, battle/main/misc17 func_80092B74, 64.13 -> 87.17% in one edit, 1252 bytes against retail's 1336 under XenoBattle260). Retail's third argument is `i * 0x20 + 0x2A` but no multiply appears: it is `lui s1,(0x2A0000 >> 16)`, `sra a2,s1,0x10` at the call and `lui v0,0x20 / addu s1,s1,v0` in the latch, and the second argument's row is the same with 0x1A0000 in its own register. Writing `s32 x1, x2; x1 = 0x2A << 16; x2 = 0x1A << 16;` then passing `x1 >> 16` / `x2 >> 16` and stepping both with `x += 0x20 << 16` in the loop body reproduces all of it (the SImode `i * 0x20 + 0x2A` gives `li`/`sll`/`ori` instead and parks in the 64-72% band). The idiom was already matched elsewhere in the overlay: battle/main/misc14's BattleBuildFlaggedSlotSpriteColumn does exactly this with `s32 pitch = 0x6E << 16` / `pitch >> 16` / `pitch += 0xA0000`. Reach for it whenever a scaled x/y argument in a loop shows a bare `sra ... ,0x10` or a `lui` of a small constant times 0x10000.
- id: g-one-constant-in-the-preheader-and | keywords: loop, invariant, hoist, gcse, li, constant, rematerialise, reassign
  **A LOOP-INVARIANT VALUE THE BODY RE-ASSIGNS IS HELD IN ONE REGISTER ACROSS THE WHOLE LOOP WHILE A CONSTANT EXPRESSION OF IT IS RE-MATERIALISED PER ITERATION - that is the only way to get one `li` in the preheader and a different one inside the loop** (round 418, battle/main/misc11 func_8007FBE0, parked 99.39% at retail's exact 264 bytes / 66 insns). Retail fills a PSX prim's four halfwords with `0xC` (from a preheader `li t2,0xC`) and `0x12` (from a `li v1,0x12` INSIDE the loop) in two different registers. Spelling both as a constant relation - `x = 0xC;` before the loop and `x + 6` at the second store - lets GCSE hoist BOTH into registers (94.39%, still 66 insns, 26 differing lines), while the reassignment form `... = (x = 0x12)` reproduces retail's two shapes exactly and leaves only an allocator tie on x's register (a 6x quotient gap, so not closable). Read a preheader `li` plus an in-loop `li` of a related value as one variable the body writes, not as two literals.

- id: a-block-move-limit-as-a-third-giv | keywords: giv, strength, reduction, block, memcpy, struct, copy, frame
  **A TRAILING `pPoly[1] = pPoly[0]` STRUCT COPY MAKES loop.c GIVE THE BLOCK MOVE'S LIMIT ITS OWN GIV, AND THAT ONE REGISTER IS THE WHOLE RESIDUAL** (round 413, field/main/misc4 func_8007A5C4, parked 77.28%, 568 bytes vs retail's 560). Retail keeps exactly two givs off the loop pointer - the Quad (+0x70) and `&quad->polys[0]` (+0x70) - and recomputes `addiu t0,s1,32` from the second inside the loop; our compile makes the copy's limit (`quad+0x40`) a third giv, bases the other stores off it with negative offsets and needs a 10th callee-saved slot (frame 0x58 vs 0x50, the uv+7 pointer spilled). The copy itself is right: 0x28 bytes as a 4-word loop plus an 8-byte tail, retail's exact shape. Tried, all 77.28 or worse with the same code: `pQuad->polys[0]` inline, a `POLY_FT4* pPoly` local, a `POLY_FT4* pNextPoly` for the copy, a `SVECTOR* pVertex` for the vertices, indexing `D_800B0FEC[i]` (73.51), `vy=0` before `vx` (73.31). A 4-minute permuter run reached 81.72 by taking `&pQuad` in one vertex store: that puts the loop pointer in memory, so gcc stops strength-reducing it and derives `&polys[0]` from the reload each iteration - a different body, not retail's, and not source anyone would write. Park rather than permute if a struct copy at the end of a loop body is the only residual. **Round 426 corrected the mechanism:** the limit giv is not the cause, it is the LAST link of one `combine_givs` chain - deleting the copy alone re-bases all twelve vertex stores onto `quad+0x2E` (the `pPoly->clut` store, the last address the loop assigns) with negative displacements, so gcc anchors the whole quad-relative address set on the LAST address recorded and retail's `add_val 0` quad base plus a second chain at `+0x20` is what this C cannot express (`pPoly` folds into `quad + 0x20`, so every address in the loop shares one chain). Also measured: retail's `vy = 0` FIRST store order 73.31 / 572 bytes / 143 insns, and the eight inline uv arguments replaced by `u_char* pUV = &D_800ADE70[i * 8];` + `pUV[0..7]` 78.04 / frame 0x48 (one saved register fewer but the second ADE70 pointer is lost, so it is further from retail than the 77.28 body). gcc-2.6.0 is 73.31 on the vy-first body and compile-fails on the parked one; 2.7.2-cdk compile-fails.
- id: g-index-form-makes-the-field-givs | keywords: induction, giv, pointer, walk, index, struct, loop, field, address
  **A STRUCT-ARRAY WALK WRITTEN AS `pRect++` WITH `pRect->field` MAKES `loop.c` KEEP ONE POINTER WITH DISPLACEMENTS AND NO FIELD GIVS, WHILE `arr[i].field` MAKES ONE GIV PER FIELD - so when retail's loop shows a register per field (`addiu s3,s3,8` written back four times at the loop bottom) the source is the INDEX form and a pointer-walk rewrite measurably destroys the shape** (round 411, field/main/misc9 func_800AB808, parked; the shape question, not the match). Retail holds four outer field walkers (`&h`, `&y`, `&w` and `&x` in a stack slot), each `addiu +8` at the outer bottom, plus inner recomputations, and spills `i`, `&x`, `pFile` and `pImage` into a 0x80 frame. Measured on the same body: the index form 68.25% / 592 bytes / 148 insns with three field givs (h, x, y) plus `i*8`; the pointer walk 68.26% / 528 bytes / 132 insns with ZERO field givs (every access becomes `lh -4(s1)`/`lh 0(s3)` off one walker), and taking the pointer per iteration for the prect stores only, inner loop still indexed, 63.69% / 576 bytes / 144 insns. So the fuzzy score can be flat (68.25 vs 68.26) across two bodies whose loop-state register sets differ completely: judge this class by the giv/increment rows, not the percentage, and never convert an index-form loop to a pointer walk to raise it. Preset closed with try_flags.sh --sweep (2.6.0 / 2.7.2 / 2.7.2-cdk x -O1/-O2/-O3 x -G4/-G8 x -mcpu 3000/r8000) plus cdk -G0 and 2.6.0 -G0: Default -O2 is the maximum and -G0 == -G8 whenever the base symbol is a plain global, so a giv-count residual of this kind is a `loop.c` heuristic, not a preset.

- id: g-wait-loop-tests-the-status | keywords: loop, volatile, status, wait, inverted, branch, sense, register
  **A GPU/DEVICE WAIT LOOP IS `while (!(status & bit))` - the loop exits when the BIT IS SET - so a park written with the test uninverted matches the branch senses mirrored and cannot reach 100 whatever else is right; the tell is retail's `bnez v0,<past the loop>` on the entry test and `beqz v0,<loop top>` at the bottom** (round 420, slus_006.64/psyq/libgpu_b LoadImage func_800460A0 98.36 -> 100.00 and its twin StoreImage func_800462DC, PSY-Q preset). The park had `while (*g_GpuGp1Port & 0x04000000) { if (func_80046F30()) return -1; }`, which is also the wrong semantics (it gives up as soon as the GPU asks for DMA); inverting it closes both the two branch senses and, with the divide-into-`size` fix above, took each of the two stubs from a parked 98.36 / 97.80 to 100.00 at retail's exact byte count. Read a wait loop's polarity off the entry branch: `bnez` to PAST the loop means the loop runs while the bit is CLEAR.

- id: g-front-end-statement-places-a-preheader | keywords: preheader, invariant, li, constant, loop, cse, placement
  **A SOURCE STATEMENT CREATING A CONSTANT MATERIALISES IT AT THE TOP OF THE LOOP PREHEADER, BEFORE THE ASSIGNMENTS WRITTEN ABOVE IT, WHEREAS THE SAME CONSTANT SHARED BY cse AND HOISTED BY `loop.c` LANDS AT THE END - so a retail preheader `li sN,K` that sits FIRST is a front-end statement, and spelling that constant as a named local reproduces its slot; the price is that cse then stops sharing the register with a `case K` compare** (round 423, `movie/main/misc1` func_800704E8, 569 insns, XenoBattle260Div, parked 99.04 -> 99.28% at retail's exact 2276 bytes). Retail's preheader is `li s3,1 / la s4,g_MovieStreamReadCount / move s2,s1`; with the constant only appearing inside the loop body (`1 - g_MovieDebugBufferParity`, two `isbg = 1` stores, `switch` `case 1`) cc1 emits `la s4 / move s2 / li s3` and no arrangement of the two assimilations reaches retail's group order (earlier rounds measured writing the assignments inside the loop, reordering them, and a `do{}while(0)` barrier). Adding `s32 isbg; ... isbg = 1;` as a statement right after the preceding call and reading it in the parity computation and the two stores moves the `li` into retail's slot exactly - the same rule the `b-strength-reduce-giv-is-always-first` entry states from the other end (front end first, loop.c after). What it costs: the switch's `case 1` test then materialises its own `li v0,1 / beq v1,v0` where retail reuses `beq v1,s3`, because the constant's register equivalence is flushed at the loop-start label; going back to `1 - x` restores the compare but returns the `li` to the end of the preheader and costs 12 bytes (2288 / 572 insns / 98.69, measured). So treat the two rows as ONE trade and probe the shape with `cc1_probe.sh` (a standalone copy of the preheader + switch shows `li $16,1` then `beq $3,$16` when both are in one extended block, which is why the loop case loses it). Not a preset question: 99.02 under gcc-2.7.2-psx, 82.76 under cdk.

  **UPDATE round 436: the trade in this entry is CLOSED - move ALL the loop-invariant statements whose hoists retail shows into the loop** (movie/main/misc1 func_800704E8, 99.28 -> 100.00 / `check_bin` OK at 2276 bytes / 569 insns). With `isbg = 1;` alone in the loop, loop.c hoists the `li` to the END of the preheader (after `la s4`/`move s2`, i.e. one row off); with `isbg = 1; pCounters = g_MovieStreamReadCount; pBase = pWork;` as the loop's first three statements, IN THAT ORDER, loop.c emits retail's whole preheader group `li s3,1 / la s4 / move s2` AND rewrites the in-loop `case 1` compare to `beq v1,s3`, so the "cse flushes the constant at the loop-start label" price does not apply to a hoisted one. Rule: a preheader whose instructions are all of one kind (front-end `li` first, or all loop.c insertions at the end) tells you which of them the original wrote INSIDE the loop; the group's ORDER is the source order of the in-loop statements.

- id: g-flat-pair-index-form-gives-the-two-givs | keywords: loop, giv, induction, sentinel, index, struct, pair, cse
  **AN INTERLEAVED `{s16,s16}` PAIR TABLE WALKED WITH THE FLAT INDEXES (`ids[i * 2]`, `ids[i * 2 + 1]`) GIVES RETAIL'S TWO INDEPENDENT INDUCTION POINTERS, WHILE THE TYPED STRUCT-ARRAY FORM (`pairs[i].parent`, `pairs[i].child`) MAKES cc1 KEEP THE INDEX AND RE-FORM THE SECOND FIELD AS A SYMBOLIC `D_...A2` + VARIABLE INDEX** (round 424, world_map/main/misc9 func_8008868C, `extern s16 ids[]; while (ids[i * 2] != -1) { WorldMapSetSpriteSetParent(base + ids[i * 2], base + ids[i * 2 + 1]); i++; }` = 100.00% at retail's exact 148 bytes / 37 insns, XenoWorldMap). Retail has `move s0,base` / `addiu s1,base,2` then `lh a0,0(s0) / addiu s0,s0,4 / lh a1,0(s1) / addiu s1,s1,4` - two givs, one per address expression. Measured on the same function: the struct-array form 69.89% / 168 bytes / 42 insns (second field loaded as `lui at,%hi(D_*A2) / addu at,at,i*?`), and the two-`s16*`-pointer walk (`pX = ids; pY = ids + 1; if (*pX != -1) do { ...; pX += 2; pY += 2; } while (*pX != -1);`) 76.81% / 152 bytes, because there both -1 compares cse to one register. **This corrects g-sentinel-guard-must-deref:** that entry concluded "no spelling breaks the sentinel's cse, park on this shape" - the index form does, because the guard's and the body's element loads are then one MEM with two different pseudos (`i`, `i + 1`), so the loop's -1 stays a bare CONST_INT. Read a sentinel loop's `.s` for TWO walkers before writing two pointer variables.

- id: g-source-walkers-give-the-field-givs | keywords: loop, giv, walker, pointer, struct, array, prologue, frame
  **FOR A `RECT`-SHAPED TABLE (FOUR s16 FIELDS AT +0/+2/+4/+6, STRIDE 8) READ INSIDE A NESTED LOOP, THE FOUR SOURCE-LEVEL FIELD WALKERS (`pH = &t[0].h; pW = &t[0].w; pY = &t[0].y; pRect = &t[0];` incremented at the loop bottom, plus a separate `s32 offset` = slot*8) ARE WORTH +9 POINTS OVER THE FULLY INDEXED BODY AND ARE THE ONLY FORM THAT REPRODUCES RETAIL'S PROLOGUE** (round 426, field/main/misc9 func_800AB808, parked 68.25 -> 77.38% at 572 bytes / 143 insns against retail's 668 / 164; Default preset confirmed by a full `try_flags.sh --sweep`). Retail keeps one walking register per field (`s3`=&h, `s7`=&y, `s8`=&w, the &x walker spilled to 0x48), spills `i` (0x38), pFile (0x28) and pImage (0x30) and runs a 0x80 frame; with the body written as `g_FieldSpriteSlotRects[slot].h/.w/.y/.x` everywhere the prologue differs from instruction 1 (frame 0x58, pFile/pImage kept in registers). The walker spellings make the first 38 instructions identical, including the two spills and the ten saved registers. The reader `memcpy` source stays indexed (`g_FieldSpriteSlotRects[slot].x / 4`), which is also retail's `symbol + offset` form. Residual: retail's frame is still 0x80 vs 0x68 because it keeps one MORE value live than this cc1 does - a second address for the inner-loop `h` test (`s4 = offset + &h` alongside the &h walker), preheader copies of the &y and &w walkers (`move s6,s7`, `move s2,s8`), and `tim.prect` re-loaded from its stack slot before each of the four store sites. Measured on that shape and dead: the inner test as `t[slot].h` instead of `*pH` (75.69 / 147 insns), dropping the pH walker and indexing `h` at both uses (77.16 / 145), a single `offset/8` counter instead of separate `slot` and `offset` (73.18), the pointer walk with all fields through one `pRect++` (68.26 / 132 - cc1 then merges every walker into displacements off one base), and `while` instead of `do{}while` for the outer loop. So when a struct-array loop's diff opens with a frame size and a spill list that are all wrong, try the per-field walker locals before typing the access.

- id: g-structbase-index-form-keeps-one-vector-walker | keywords: loop, walker, induction, vector, increment, split, delay-slot, struct
  **ZEROING A STRUCT FIELD AND PASSING ITS ADDRESS SPLITS INTO TWO INDUCTION REGISTERS 8 APART WITH `pVector++`, BUT KEEPS ONE WALKER WHEN BOTH USES ARE WRITTEN AS THE SAME `&pSlots->vectors[i]` INDEX EXPRESSION** (round 438, field/main/main func_80077AB4, parked; Default preset). Retail zeroes `sh $zero,0/2/4($s2)` off ONE register and does `addiu s2,s2,8` in the `jal` delay slot; the `pVector->vx = 0; ... func(..., pVector++);` body emits `sh $zero,0($sN)` off the pre-increment pointer and `sh $zero,-2($sM)` / `0($sM)` off the post-increment one - two callee-saved registers for one pointer. Writing the three stores as `pSlots->vectors[i].vx/vy/vz` and the argument as `&pSlots->vectors[i]` gives retail's single walker plus the delay-slot addiu. Worth 3.5 points on the same body (75.93 vs 72.42 under the array-decl form of that unit's count global). The same body's remaining residue is a MULT giv, not this: see `g-qimode-index-blocks-the-mult-giv`.- id: g-conditional-const-pair-folds-away-the-third-constant-use | keywords: loop, hoist, constant, callee-saved, frame, ternary, fold, negu
  **A SMALL-CONSTANT LOOP INVARIANT THAT RETAIL KEEPS IN A CALLEE-SAVED REGISTER NEEDS THREE REGISTER USES INSIDE THE LOOP, AND `if (c) x = A; else x = B;` (OR THE TERNARY) BETWEEN TWO CONSTANTS FOLDS TO ARITHMETIC, WHICH REMOVES ONE OF THEM - no spelling tried avoids the fold under gcc-2.6.0** (round 429, menu/main/misc8_r2 func_801D9F98, re-measured 95.67% / 1288 bytes / 322 insns against retail's 1296 / 324, XenoMenu260 = gcc-2.6.0). Retail's three in-loop uses of 2 are all register compares (`beq v1,s5` at the switch root, `beq v0,s5` at the choice site, `beq v0,s5` at the case-4 test), plus its `ori s5,zero,0x2` preheader load and the `$s5` save/restore pair that makes the frame 0x48 instead of 0x40; our body can only produce TWO register compares because the choice site becomes `xori a0,a0,0x2; sltiu a0,a0,1; negu a0,a0; andi a0,a0,0x7` (= `7 & -(x == 2)`), and at two uses loop.c never pays for the move (preheader ori + save + restore = 3 insns breaks even at three uses). Measured byte-identical at 95.67 / 322, each: `choice = 7; if (x != 2) choice = 0;`, `if (x == 2) choice = 7; else choice = 0;`, the ternary passed straight to the call (`f(x == 2 ? 7 : 0)`), and a `u_char choice` (that one adds a surplus `andi a0,a0,0x7`). So the saved-set diff and the fold are ONE residual, not two: when retail compares against a register holding a small constant and your body has an `xori/sltiu/negu/andi` chain at one site, retail's constant was hoisted and that fold is why yours never is. Also screened on the same body and dead: `-O3` 82.35, gcc-2.7.2-cdk `-G0` 78.56, gcc-2.7.2-cdk `-G8` compile-failed.
  **ZEROING A STRUCT FIELD AND PASSING ITS ADDRESS SPLITS INTO TWO INDUCTION REGISTERS 8 APART WITH `pVector++`, BUT KEEPS ONE WALKER WHEN BOTH USES ARE WRITTEN AS THE SAME `&pSlots->vectors[i]` INDEX EXPRESSION** (round 438, field/main/main func_80077AB4, parked; Default preset). Retail zeroes `sh $zero,0/2/4($s2)` off ONE register and does `addiu s2,s2,8` in the `jal` delay slot; the `pVector->vx = 0; ... func(..., pVector++);` body emits `sh $zero,0($sN)` off the pre-increment pointer and `sh $zero,-2($sM)` / `0($sM)` off the post-increment one - two callee-saved registers for one pointer. Writing the three stores as `pSlots->vectors[i].vx/vy/vz` and the argument as `&pSlots->vectors[i]` gives retail's single walker plus the delay-slot addiu. Worth 3.5 points on the same body (75.93 vs 72.42 under the array-decl form of that unit's count global). The same body's remaining residue is a MULT giv, not this: see `g-qimode-index-blocks-the-mult-giv`.
- id: g-op-histogram-tells-a-cse-residual-from-a-missing-binding | keywords: loop, cse, andi, ori, histogram, residual, register, pressure, park
  **COUNT OPCODES PER LOOP BODY ON BOTH SIDES BEFORE HUNTING A SPELLING: WHEN THE BRANCH, `lhu` AND `sh` COUNTS MATCH AND ONLY `andi`/`ori`/`or` DIFFER, NOTHING IS MISSING FROM THE SOURCE - retail's compile did LESS common-subexpression elimination than yours, so the gap is register pressure and the body is ready to park** (round 444, slus_006.64/system/system func_80034FFC, 424 retail insns, parked 66.79%; the method is what the park note records). Retail's plane-0 loop is 156 insns and its compile is 126: andi 37 against 24, ori 26 against 22, or 12 against 9, sltu+negu 4 against 2, but branches 27 against 27, lhu 13 against 13, sh 12 against 12 - retail re-forms every `word & bit` test (its `$v0` is clobbered by the next test) and re-derives each `(word & 0x80) ? C : 0` bool, while ours keeps `word & 0x10` in a register across two columns. Extract both histograms in one command each: retail's body from the address range in `asm/<bin>/nonmatchings/<unit>/<Func>.s` (`/* OFFSET */` column), yours from the generated `build/src/<unit>.c.s` between the loop label and its backward branch, skipping `.set`/labels/`#nop`. This separates "my body is short an operation" (a load, store or call count differs - keep hunting) from "the compiler deduplicated more here" (park), which the objdiff percentage alone cannot tell you.
- id: g-three-incremented-pointer-locals-mint-a-plus4-giv-per-base | keywords: loop, giv, induction, pointer, frame, callee-saved, increment, spill
  **THREE POINTER LOCALS INCREMENTED IN THE `for`-UPDATE MAKE cc1 MINT ONE DERIVED GIV PER BASE (`pA+4`, `pB+4`, `pC+4`), SO THE LOOP CARRIES SIX REGISTERS INSTEAD OF FOUR AND THE FUNCTION GROWS A FRAME AND A CALLEE-SAVED SAVE - and the two cheaper spellings of the same loop body are measured too** (round 444, slus_006.64/system/system func_80034FFC: writing a 3-word stride walk as `pAbove`/`pBelow`/`pDest` all advanced in the `for`-update scored 60.46% / 413 insns / 1652 bytes with an 8-byte frame and `$16` saved; assigning `pAbove`/`pBelow` from `pDest` at the top of the body instead scored 64.92% / 397 / 1588 with no frame, and leaving them as address expressions `(pDest - rowStride)[k]` / `(pDest + rowStride)[k]` off the one advanced pointer scored 66.79% / 391 / 1564 with no frame - retail is 424 / 1696 with four increments and no frame). So when retail's loop increments three row pointers plus the base and shows only ONE `+4` giv, the source did not hold all three as locals: reach for `g-op-histogram-tells-a-cse-residual-from-a-missing-binding` before spending runs on pointer spellings, and note that `rowStride` scaled in two separate places (retail `sll v1,a3,16 / sra v1,v1,16 / sll v0,v1,1` for one pointer, a second `sll v1,v1,1` for the other) is the shape loop.c's giv addends take - folding both into one `sra 15` is what the expression form does.

- id: g-a-mult-after-the-jal-is-an-inlined-expression-not-a-giv | keywords: loop, hoist, cse, call, mult, spill, frame, expression
  **A `mult` THAT RETAIL RE-EXECUTES INSIDE EACH ITERATION OF A LOOP WHOSE BODY CONTAINS A `jal` IS THE SOURCE WRITING THE EXPRESSION IN THE STORE STATEMENTS, NOT A HOISTED LOCAL: gcc cannot CSE a value across a call, so `nV = texY + rowStep * (j + 1);` before the loop is computed ONCE while `pPoly->v2 = texY + rowStep * (j + 1);` gives retail's per-iteration `mult/mflo/addu` - but writing it inline also makes retail's registers (texX*4, texY, the running end value, clut) stay live across every call, and forcing the inline form onto a body that had hoisted them RAISES pressure instead of lowering it** (round 446, lib_gear/main/main func_801E1A14, re-parked 76.11% / 2172 bytes / 543 insns against retail's 2276 / 569, XenoLibraryDiv). Read the two shapes as a set: retail's outer segment loop is 147 insns and the hoisted body 133, the difference being 2 extra static mults (v2 in loop 1, v0/v1 in loop 2), EIGHT words spilled around each `SetPolyGT3` against six (the extra two are the invariant registers), a second induction pointer `s1 = pSeg + k * 0x28` (field stores at disp 0x14..0x2D) where the hoisted body uses one base at `pSeg + 8 + k * 0x28` (disp 0xC..0x25), and a `addu a1,t6,zero` copy of the multiplicand. Measured on that body: dropping the `nV1` local and writing the expression inline in both k-loops gave 16.57% / 2268 bytes / 567 insns - retail's instruction COUNT to within 2 - but the frame grew 0x130 -> 0x140 and the whole allocation rotated, i.e. the reading is right and the pressure is the wall. Also measured on the same body and dead: `pSeg->polys[k].field` instead of the `pPoly` local (75.25 / 2176 / 544) - the local is not what anchors the `+8` in the base. So decode the store-by-store semantics FIRST (both k-loops there store retail's exact values) and park on the hoist/pressure statement rather than hunting the `+8`.

- id: g-movable-copy-hoist-is-context-not-spelling | keywords: loop, hoist, move, copy, preheader, latch, movable, body
  **A LOOP-ARGUMENT COPY THAT RETAIL KEEPS IN THE BODY (`addu a1,s1,zero` right after the loop label) BUT YOUR COMPILE HOISTS INTO A PREHEADER COPY PLUS A RE-COPY AFTER THE BIV INCREMENT (in the back-edge delay slot) IS A loop.c move_movables DECISION ABOUT THE LOOP'S SURROUNDING RTL, NOT ABOUT THE LOOP - so no spelling of that loop, its bound or its counter reaches it** (round 454, field/main/misc2 func_80074108, re-parked 97.44% / 1432 bytes / 358 insns against retail's 357, one instruction over and so never linkable, XenoFieldNoGp). Retail has three such loops and the same body reproduces two of them (loop 1 body copy, loop 2 init+latch) while only loop 3 hoists. Measured dead on it, each 358 insns: changing loop 1's bound to 2 iterations (0x16) and to 4 iterations (0x18, loop 3's exact trip count) leaves ITS body copy in place, so the movable's iteration-count savings are ruled out; a fresh counter pseudo for loop 3 (`k` instead of the shared `i`) still hoists (96.42); and the four pointer/index spellings in the park note all hoist. `permute.sh` cannot run on such a body when it holds a struct-typed cast the permuter's parser rejects (`conversion to non-scalar type requested` on the preprocessed base), so the park gets no permuter pass until the body is simplified.

## store ordering and volatile
- id: h-a-bitfield-clear-run-goes-where-the-loop-source-says | keywords: store, order, bitfield, loop, read-modify-write, schedule, mask
  **A `u_int` BITFIELD RUN IN A LOOP BODY FOLLOWS SOURCE ORDER, NOT REORG: the plain field write (`scripts[i].flags_0 = 0xFFFF;`) written BEFORE the `state`/`isInUse`/`flags_0x17`/`flags_0x12` clears splits the read-modify-write of the same word and emits the halfword store FIRST, while writing it LAST emits retail's `lw word / and,and,and / or / sw word / sh word` order** (round 441, field/main/misc8 func_80080A74: 82.88 -> 83.17, 1228 bytes / 307 insns, the only edit of four that moved the score). So on a loop whose word RMW and its sibling wide/narrow stores differ only in order, re-order the SOURCE statements of the same word before assuming the scheduler did it.

- id: h-a-do-while-in-both-arms-pins-a-shared-tails-argument-setup | keywords: store, order, barrier, cross-jump, tail, argument, delay, slot
  **A SHARED TAIL REACHED FROM TWO if/else ARMS LOSES ITS FIRST INSTRUCTION'S MERGE - retail's tail begins with the call's argument setup (`j tail` + `nop`, then `jal BattleSetScreenMode` with `addu a0,zero,zero` in the slot) while the compile emits `j tail` + `move a0,zero`, a second `move a0,zero` at the end of the other arm and a `nop` in the slot (204 bytes against retail's 200) - and AN EMPTY `do { } while (0);` BETWEEN THE LAST ARM STORE AND THE FIRST TAIL STATEMENT IN **BOTH** ARMS IS THE WHOLE FIX** (round 452, battling/main/misc8 func_80080C48, 95.60 -> 100.00% MATCH at retail's exact 200 bytes / 50 insns, Default preset). Duplicating the tail in both arms already reproduces retail's block order `[test1][A][j tail][test2][B][tail][else]` (tail-once is 79.80, a switch 66.70, `s32 mode = 0` for the argument no help, a 3-minute permuter run base=221 best=221 - the park had all five), and the barrier in only ONE arm measures 92.70, so the two barriers are both load-bearing: they stop sched1/reorg hoisting the constant out of each arm's block, which is what keeps it inside the region cross-jump merges. Same mechanism as `h-an-empty-do-while-pins-a-run-of-stores` / `n-an-empty-statement-between`, read on a cross-jumped tail.

- id: h-a-three-group-tail-rotates-under-source-order | keywords: store, order, three, group, gt, ptr, tail, rotate
  **IN gcc-2.7.2 A STRAIGHT-LINE TAIL OF THREE INDEPENDENT STATEMENT GROUPS COMES OUT AS `(source 1, source 3, source 2)` - THE MIDDLE STATEMENT'S GROUP IS EMITTED LAST - so when retail's order is `(a, b, c)` the source that emits it is `(a, c, b)`** (round 443, field/main/misc7 func_80099214, Default preset; the park went 96.45 -> 97.91 on this single edit). The tail is the walk-to-position rebuild exit: two flag-word read-modify-writes (`scriptFlags.flags &= ~0x10000;`, `flags &= ~0x200000;`) and the `scriptInstructionPointer += 0xD` halfword bump; retail emits `lw 0/sw 0/lhu 204/addiu/sh 204/lw 4/and/sw 4`, i.e. flags0, ptr, flags4. Written ptr-first (the natural reading order: advance then clear) the compile rotated to ptr, flags4, flags0 (96.45); written flags0, ptr, flags4 it rotated to flags0, flags4, ptr (96.26); written flags0, flags4, ptr it emits retail's exact three groups (97.91, 153 -> 141 differing lines). So on a tail whose groups are all instruction-identical and only rotated, try all three source permutations - the pair of measured rotations is enough to solve for the third, and sched2 was not involved.


symptoms: stores in another order, reload of a global, volatile
try: store volatile reload order
- id: c-a-copy-block-from-element-four-is-a-pointer-walk | keywords: copy, halfword, block, pointer, walk, addiu, u16, globals
  **SIX HALFWORD COPIES WHOSE SECOND GROUP RETAIL REACHES WITH AN EXTRA `addiu vN,vN,0x8` AND THEN LOADS AT 0/2/4 ARE A POINTER WALK FROM ELEMENT 4, NOT `arr[4]/[5]/[6]`**: `p = g_States + 4; dst[0] = p[0]; dst[1] = p[1]; dst[2] = p[2];` reproduces retail's `lui/addiu` + `addiu v0,v0,8` + the three 0/2/4 load/store pairs, and the indexed spelling costs exactly that instruction (it folds the offsets into 8/0xA/0xC instead) - which shows up as one instruction short and 3 points low, and would shift the whole overlay if converted (round 439, battle/main/misc30 func_800B905C: 94.47% / 508 bytes -> 96.61% / 512, and the same `pStates = g_BattleAnimGroupStates + 4; pStates[1..2]` idiom is in misc31's MATCHED code, i.e. read the unit's neighbours for the walk). With that fixed the block's remaining residual was only which base pair holds `$v0`/`$v1`, which the hoisted-compare lever above then closed by landing the exact 508 bytes.

- id: h-an-empty-do-while-pins-a-run-of-stores | keywords: store, order, sink, barrier, loop-note, do-while, scheduler, arms
  **A RUN OF PLAIN STORES THAT gcc SINKS BELOW THE VOLATILE STORES THAT FOLLOW THEM IN SOURCE IS PINNED BACK BY AN EMPTY `do { } while (0);` AFTER THE RUN - the loop note is a scheduling boundary, and this works where `volatile` cannot (the run's stores are to a parameter's struct and the volatile ones are globals)** (round 430, world_map/main/misc7 func_800828DC, 74.00 -> 76.40% and 461 -> 427 differing instructions, XenoWorldMapDiv preset, still parked at 75.62 / 1664 bytes / 416 insns against retail's 1672 / 418). The body is a command/state machine: each of the five big arms writes `command = 0; state = N; unk5C = <const>;` then three volatile rotation stores, and cc1 emitted the three plain stores AFTER the two volatile ones and hoisted the `lh` reload of the just-stored global into the gap. Adding `do { } while (0);` after the `unk5C` store in each arm put them back in retail's order; a second barrier after the ViewRotationZ store also stopped the `lh` hoist. So when an arm's stores come out rotated and the tail of the arm is already right, try the empty do/while between store groups before re-ordering the source (the same barrier is documented in this unit's WorldMapBeginSlotAnimFrame for a load, and in g-switch-dispatch-makes-loop for a dispatch).
- id: h-a-do-while-group-pins-a-scheduled-store-block | keywords: store, order, do-while, barrier, scheduler, block, register, 260
  **A `do { one group of stores } while (0)` PER GROUP PINS A SCHEDULED STRAIGHT-LINE BLOCK'S ORDER IN A XENOBATTLE260 UNIT (plain -O2, no -fschedule-insns in the flags) AND MOVES THE BLOCK'S POINTER INTO RETAIL'S REGISTER - so the ordering rows of such a diff are block boundaries, not statement positions** (round 433, battle/main/misc3 func_80077990, 92.19 -> 92.83% and 97 -> 61 differing lines, still parked at 1820 bytes / 455 insns against retail's 1816 / 454). The proof that this unit schedules at all is cc1's own `build/src/<unit>.c.s`: the same C emits each rect's `lhu` above the neighbouring constant stores and its `andi/sll/sh` behind them, where retail's head is pure RTL order (value-then-address per statement, no store crossing another). Wrapping each rect's `x = ...` and the `w/h/y` trio that follows it in their own `do { } while (0)` put the stores back in retail's per-group order AND gave `pRects` `$v1` / the coords pointer `$a1` exactly as retail has them (before that they were `$a0`/`$a1`). Two measured refinements: a block PER STATEMENT instead of per group compiles BYTE-IDENTICALLY (only the group boundary is a lever), and a matched extension of this lever is the empty `do { } while (0);` of h-an-empty-do-while-pins-a-run-of-stores. Not reachable this way on the same body: retail's `li v0,1` / `li v1,0xC6` placed before the block's pointer load, retail's first `lhu` above a four-store group, and retail's StoreImage argument setup (`addu a0,s0,s7` / `move a1,s6`) sitting above the eight rect stores - that last one is a scheduler hoist with no C statement behind it.
  COUNTER-CASE with the EMPTY-barrier form (round 434, field/main/misc9 func_800AC3AC, a
  56-store POLY_GT4 head): one `do { } while (0);` after each of the five constant groups
  collapsed the body from 380 to 333 instructions and 97.89% to 71.92 / 1332 bytes - cc1
  merged stores across the barriers instead of pinning them - so the barrier family is not
  free on a body whose stores are already grouped by constant; measure it before trusting it.
- id: h-a-join-store-of-arms-that-store-different-values-is-one-source-store | keywords: store, join, cross-jump, value, arms, constant, shared, tie
  **THE COUNTERPART OF `h-one-retail-store-at-a-join-is-a-cross-jumped-per-arm-store`: WHEN THE ARMS STORE DIFFERENT VALUES gcc 2.7.2 DOES NOT CROSS-JUMP THEM, SO A RETAIL STORE AT THE JOIN MEANS THE ORIGINAL HAD ONE STORE STATEMENT BELOW THE JOIN (a value temp), AND THE PER-ARM SPELLING SCORES HIGHER WHILE BEING STRUCTURALLY WRONG - take the lower percentage; it is the one with retail's bytes** (round 437, field/main/misc10 func_8009635C, Default preset, 176 bytes / 44 insns = retail's exact size in BOTH bodies, still parked on register allocation). Retail is `beq s0,-1,new / addu v1,q+n / lbu a0,0(v1) / sltiu v0,a0,99 / beqz v0,exit / nop / j JOIN / addiu v0,a0,1` and `new: ... sb s1,0(v0) / li v0,1` then `JOIN: sb v0,0(v1)`, i.e. one store reached by both arms with the address register live from the load. Per-arm stores (`if (q < 99) q++;` vs `q = 1;`) measure 96.70% and reorg fills the `j`'s delay slot with path 1's `sb` (blocks not merged, because the two stores' values are a register and a `const_int`). Writing the quantity into a temp in each arm and ONE `pQuantities[nIndex] = nQuantity;` after the if/else reproduces retail's block layout and instruction count exactly (90.41%, everything else register-only). So judge such a park by the store COUNT in the `.s`, not by objdiff: the higher-scoring body was the wrong shape. On the `q < 99` test, `nQuantity = pQuantities[nIndex]; if (nQuantity < 99) nQuantity++;` gives `slti` (89.16%) - the comparison has to be written on the byte, `pQuantities[nIndex] < 99`, for retail's `sltiu`.
- id: h-a-volatile-store-defeats-store-to-load-folding-keeping-the-symbol | keywords: volatile, store, fold, lh, symbol, relocation, constant, read
  **A CONSTANT STORED TO A HALFWORD GLOBAL AND READ BACK A FEW STATEMENTS LATER IS FORWARDED BY cse (the store's value replaces the load, and the `lui`/`lh`/`sll` retail has disappear), and the cheap escape is a VOLATILE STORE with a PLAIN READ: `*(volatile u16 *)&g_X = 0x400;` then `v = (s16)g_X;` keeps retail's `lh %lo(g_X)` AND ITS OWN SYMBOL, where the older escape (reading through a neighbouring symbol with a `[1]`/`[2]` subscript) leaves a base+addend relocation that diff_func reports as a mismatch forever** (round 443, world_map/main/misc8 func_8008390C, XenoWorldMapDiv, park 96.15 -> 96.48% at retail's exact 244 bytes / 61 insns). The two reads here are the view angles, stored a few statements earlier in the same block; making only the two STORES volatile removed 8 relocation diff lines and moved nothing else (the read still emits a real load). A volatile READ is the wrong direction - the sibling park in the same file records that spelling as worse. Same trick as the `func_80083A00` park's volatile refill, which uses it for the opposite reason (to stop cc1 folding `K << 12` into an immediate).
- id: h-volatile-store-pins-the-arg-pair | keywords: volatile, store, sched2, argument, ori, hoist, load
  **A CALL'S COMPUTED ARGUMENT (`ori a1,a2,<const>` off a base the block's stores already use) THAT RETAIL PLACES WITH THE BLOCK'S FIRST STORE AND YOURS PLACES AFTER THE BLOCK'S GLOBAL LOADS IS PINNED BY MAKING THAT FIRST STORE `volatile` - sched2 will not sink a store past, nor hoist a load past, a volatile MEM, so the pair stays at source position and the address form comes with it** (round 419, world_map/main/misc5 func_8007C3B8, 98.13 -> 99.95% at retail's EXACT 876 bytes / 219 insns, every instruction identical, XenoWorldMapDiv preset). The switch case fills a scratchpad vector and calls `WorldMapInitSpriteGroupSprites(0x14, &pScratchPos[0x50], 0)`; retail is `li a0,0x14 / ori a1,a2,0xA0 / sh zero,0xA2(a2) / lui+lw g_WorldMapCameraTarget / lui+lw ...TargetZ / sra / sra / sh 0xA0 / sh 0xA4 / jal`, and the plain body (`pScratchPos[0x51] = 0;` then the two stores) emits the SAME four instructions but with the `ori` + `sh zero` pair after the two loads, i.e. 53 differing lines and 98.13% with nothing else wrong. Writing the FIRST store as `*(volatile u16 *)&pScratchPos[0x51] = 0;` (the other two stores plain, no other change) reproduces retail's order in one edit. Measured dead on the same body at 876 bytes / 98.13: the three plain store orders (including `sh zero` last), the two shift results in function-top locals, an `u8 *` / `s16 *` / `u16 *` scratch local for the argument, one `u16 *pTilePos = &pScratchPos[0x50]` used for all three stores AND the argument (CSE folds the base back either way), and a 4-minute permuter run (2179 iterations, base=120 best=120). So when a diff's only delta is where a computed call argument sits relative to the block's loads, try the volatile cast on the store that retail keeps adjacent to it before permuting - and note the counterpart from `h-one-retail-store-at-a-join-is-a-cross-jumped-per-arm-store`: volatile on a store whose ADDRESS the pointer-iv would otherwise fold costs the body 8 bytes, so put the cast on a scratchpad or plain-base store, not on a field of an iv-walked struct. The resulting body still cannot be committed in this unit (its jump table lands 4 bytes late; lever i-jtbl-midchain-4mod8), so it is parked at 99.95.
- id: h-one-retail-store-at-a-join-is-a-cross-jumped-per-arm-store | keywords: store, order, hoist, join, arms, scheduling, cross-jump, block
  **WHEN RETAIL HAS ONE STORE AT AN IF/ELSE JOIN AND YOUR COMPILE SCHEDULES THAT SAME STORE BELOW THE NEXT STATEMENT'S ARGUMENT LOADS, the original stored in EVERY arm and cc1 cross-jumped them back into the one at the join - write the store once per arm and the block boundary reproduces retail's order with no other change** (round 418, world_map/main/misc8_b func_80085CDC, 99.18 -> 99.94% at retail's exact 636 bytes / 158 insns, XenoWorldMapDiv). The tail is a four-arm turn-step slew where every arm assigns the new step into one `d`, then `pActor->redStep = d;` and `func_800223B0(pActor->pSprite, (d - g_WorldMapViewRotationY - 0x400) & 0xFFF);`. With the store once below the join, cc1's scheduler hoists the `lw a0,0(s0)` of the callee's first argument above the store and sinks the `sw` into the `lhu` stall: `lw a0 / lui+lhu / sw / subu` where retail is `sw / lui+lhu / lw a0 / subu` - same instruction multiset, same frame, 99.18%. Repeating `pActor->redStep = d;` in all four arms gives retail's exact body: cc1's cross-jumping merges the four stores into one at the join, and the merged insn is the first of that block, so it stays ahead of the argument loads. Two things that do NOT work and are worth skipping: `*(volatile s32 *)&pActor->redStep = d;` also fixes the order but the volatile MEM is never entered in cse, so the address is re-formed off the array base instead of the pointer iv (`sw v1,92(s2)` for `sw v1,16(s0)`), a base register has to stay live across the loop, and the body grows to 648 bytes / 161 insns (95.45%); a `void *pSprite` local for the callee's first argument is copy-propagated away (identical 99.18%). The per-arm `pActor->redStep += 0x100;` / `= pActor->angle;` spelling (no `d` store) does NOT cross-jump - three separate `sw`s, 648 bytes, 93.77% - so the arms must all store the same value `d` for the merge to happen. AMENDMENT (round 449, field/main/main FieldMain, Default preset, 99.50 -> 100.00% at retail's exact 3284 bytes / 821 insns): the arms need NOT store the same value - one arm storing the constant `0xFF` and the other a global load (`lhu`) also cross-jumps into ONE `sw` at the join, and that is the fix for the mirror residual (a store at the join with an independent LOAD hoisted above it): `if (x == 0) { g_A = 0; g_B = 0xFF; } else g_B = g_C;` instead of a `bankIndex` temp and one store below, which compiles as `lw v1,g_FieldSystemMode / sw v0,g_B` where retail has `sw v0,g_B / lui v1+lw v1,g_FieldSystemMode`. Mechanism proved on the same body by compiling the unit's `.i` with the unit's cc1: `-fno-schedule-insns2` reproduces retail's order exactly, `-fno-schedule-insns` does not (so this is sched2), and neither a `*(volatile s32 *)&g_B = bankIndex;` store nor a volatile read of the loaded global stops the hoist - both are byte-identical at 99.50, so do not spend runs on the volatile barrier for a post-reload sched2 swap; the per-arm store is the lever.
- id: h-a-moved-group-of-volatile-stores | keywords: volatile, store, order, group, block, offset, diff, reassociation
  **A DIFF WHERE A WHOLE IDENTICAL RUN OF VOLATILE STORES SITS 60+ INSTRUCTIONS FROM WHERE RETAIL PUTS IT IS THE C'S STATEMENT-GROUP ORDER, NOT THE ALLOCATOR OR THE SCHEDULER - and the line diff hides it, so read the offset SEQUENCE (`sh/sb <reg>,<off>(v0)` in emitted order) instead** (round 416, battle/main/misc16 func_8008DE04, 80.93 -> 100.00 at retail's exact 1580 bytes / 395 insns, XenoBattle260). The park's body listed quad 0's corners, quad 0's texcoords, quad 1's corners, quad 1's texcoords; retail emits BOTH quads' corners (16 `sh`) and then BOTH quads' texcoords (16 `sb`), and inside one volatile group gcc emits exactly the source order. The old note had spent two rounds on a "three-register rotation" that was really this: with the groups reordered the same body scored 98.20 and the registers fell into place on their own. Two embedded-assignment locals then closed it: `(bottom = arg2 + 0xFFF8)` reproduces retail's `li 0xfff8 / addu` where a bare `arg2 - 8` folds to one `addiu`, and naming the sums `(mid = left + (half = (arg3 - 16) / 2))` / `(right = mid + half)` gives one `addu` each, where the inline `(arg1 + 8) + half` reassociates to `arg1 + (half + 8)` (`addiu v1,a3,8 / addu a1,a1,v1`). Plain statement locals before the stores hoist (the earlier 75.99 measurement); the embedded form does not, which is why both matched neighbours of this row-builder family are written that way.
- id: h-volatile-prefix-pins-an-arms | keywords: store, order, volatile, hoist, load, delay-slot, arms, switch
  **VOLATILE ON AN ARM'S FIRST STORES PINS BOTH THE STORE ORDER AND THE GLOBAL LOADS THAT FOLLOW, BUT THE ARM'S LAST STORE MUST STAY PLAIN OR IT CAN NEVER REACH THE JUMP'S DELAY SLOT - mark the prefix, leave the suffix** (round 435, world_map/main/misc6 func_8007EE34, 91.50 -> 98.17% on a 676-instruction command/state machine with three switch jump tables, XenoWorldMapDiv; still parked at 2692 bytes / 673 insns against retail's 2704 / 676). Every command arm is `command = 0; state = N; x = <recomputed from g_WorldMapPlayerPosition>; z = <recomputed>;` and cc1 hoisted the `lw g_WorldMapPlayerPositionY` and the `lw 0x40(pActor)` above the two `sh`, moved the y store above the x store, and floated the `li` of the state constant; retail keeps source order and fills the load-delay slots with the stores. Writing the prefix as `*(volatile s16 *)&pActor->command = 0; *(volatile s16 *)&pActor->state = N; *(volatile s32 *)&pActor->x = ...;` reproduced retail's order in all six arms in one edit (96.96% from 91.50), and the same marking on the two constant-only arms took it to 98.17. Making the z store volatile too REGRESSED to 2724 bytes: retail's `sw` sits in the `j` delay slot of the arm's tail jump (or in a cross-jumped shared tail), and a volatile store cannot be moved past a control transfer, so the arm grew a `nop` and the store moved up - leave the last store of a suffix-shared arm non-volatile. Two companion measurements on the same body: (a) declare per-arm scratchpad pointers INSIDE the case block, never at function scope, or their `lui/ori` pair lands in the entry block, costs a frame slot and pushes the whole body out (`pPos`/`pAngle` at function scope measured 91.50 with an extra `$s7` save); (b) a `(SVECTOR *)((u32)pScratch | 0xA0)` spelling gives retail's `ori a1,s4,0xa0` at a single-use call site where `pScratch + 0xA0` gives `addiu`; and (c) write the LAST switch's case labels in the order the machine-coded blocks appear rather than by value (0/1/16/25, then 4/17/65, then 2/3), which removes the extra `j` + `move v0,s6` a fall-through return costs (95.51 -> 96.44 in one edit). Preset ruled out on the final body: gcc-2.7.2-cdk 83.56.

- id: h-volatile-cast-restores-a-repeated-load | keywords: volatile, lbu, cse, reload, double-load, address, order
  **A RETAIL DOUBLE LOAD FROM ONE CSE'D ADDRESS (`addiu v0,v0,0x140` / `addu a0,v0,a3` / `lbu v1,0(a0)` for a compare / `lbu v0,0(a0)` for the value) IS ONLY REPRODUCIBLE BY CASTING THE LOAD `volatile`: plain C makes gcc cse the two loads into one** (round 412, battle/main/misc13 func_800877E0, 59.15 -> 60.24%, 440 -> 448 bytes against retail's 456, Default preset). When a byte from a streamed table is tested and then used twice, write `*(volatile u8 *)(base + <full index expression>)` at each of the four sites: cc1 then keeps the shared ADDRESS (the index arithmetic is still cse'd, which is why retail has one `a0`) and reloads the byte, and the per-store recompute of the whole chain comes for free because the intervening stores to the globals kill the table reads. Do NOT hoist a `u8 *pParam = <global>` local instead - gcc then hoists the whole index chain out of the loop (43.58%, 436 bytes). This buys the instruction COUNT and shape only; the residual on that function is the allocator (i/o in a2/a3 swapped), so park on it rather than permuting from 60%.
- id: h-a-pointer-local-caches-a-global-retail-reloads | keywords: reload, global, pointer, local, cse, store, alias, pointer-to-global
  **A POINTER-TO-A-GLOBAL LOCAL (`BattleSlotState *p = g_X;` used at every later site) KEEPS ONE LOAD WHERE RETAIL RELOADS THE GLOBAL POINTER AFTER EVERY INTERVENING STORE, because a store through any pointer may alias the global's storage - so spell the global expression (`g_X->field`) at each site and let cse merge only the sites no store separates** (round 421, battle/main/misc20 func_8009CBC4, 97.13 -> 100.00 at retail's exact 1936 bytes / 483 insns, XenoBattle260 gcc 2.6.0, ported from its misc17 twin). Retail loads `g_BattleTargetSlotState` once for eight consecutive effect-flag tests, then reloads it (`lui v0,%hi / lw v0,%lo`) in each of the three later tests, because each is preceded by a store (`g_BattleTargetSlotEntry->activeEffectFlags2 |= 0x40`, `g_BattleCurveTargetModes[i] = 3`, the table `sw`) and the global is a pointer variable a store could alias. Holding the pointer in a local - the twin's spelling, `pAtkState = g_BattleTargetSlotState;` - is legal and pins it in `$a1` across all eleven sites: three `lui`+`lw` pairs (6 insns / 24 bytes) disappear and the function reads 1904 against retail's 1936. Replacing the local with the direct global expression everywhere reproduced retail exactly in one edit, register `$a0` included. Check this before blaming the allocator when a diff's only quantity difference is a missing `lw` of a `g_X` pointer."

- id: h-store-then-read-back-of-a-global | keywords: store, reload, global, fold, hoist, volatile, aliasing, halfword
  **cc1 2.7.2 FORWARDS A HALFWORD STORE INTO A LATER `lh` OF THE SAME SYMBOL, SO A RETAIL SEQUENCE `sh $v0,%lo(G)` / ... / `lh $v0,%lo(G)` CANNOT BE WRITTEN BY NAMING G TWICE - and the two escapes both cost the block order** (round 409, world_map/main/misc8 func_80083A00, six switch cases each store a view-rotation halfword and then read it back to scale it by `<< 12`; parked 63.80%, 352 insns against retail's 395). Measured, all six sites the same: naming the symbol (`(s16)g_WorldMapViewRotationY << 12`) folds the read to a constant (`lui $v0,0x40`, 3 insns short per site) even with two pointer stores in between - gcc 2.7.2 does forward across an unknown-base store here, so "the stores defeat it" is false; reading through the neighbouring symbol instead (`((s16 *)&g_WorldMapCameraRot)[1]`, base+addend, the spelling that closed the sibling func_8008390C) stops the fold but cc1 then treats the two symbol_refs as distinct objects and HOISTS the `lh` above the `sh` that writes it (`lh $3,g_WorldMapCameraRot+2` before `sh $2,g_WorldMapCameraRot`), which is a stale-value reorder and silently wrong code; `*(volatile s16 *)&G` keeps the load after the store but every non-volatile store in the case block floats around the barrier (the case's `li`/`sh`/`sw` order scrambles, 59.73%) and the load comes out `lhu` + `sll 16` when the extern is `u16`. So on a diff whose only shape difference is a retail `lh` of a global your body just wrote, the fold is the wall: park with the base+addend spelling (right instruction count) rather than the symbol (right reloc) and say which. Not yet tried from this file's levers: an address-taken pointer local to the global (`h-address-taken-load-blocks-hoist` above) as the load barrier.
- id: h-independent-loads-hoist-above-the-store | keywords: store, hoist, load, order, global, inline, lbu, pointer, local
  **A PARK NOTE THAT SAYS "gcc SINKS THESE LOADS BELOW THE STORE, SO RETAIL'S LOADS-FIRST ORDER IS UNREACHABLE" WASTES A LANE: put the loading statement FIRST in source and write the store through the INLINE global expression, and cc1 hoists the store's own global-pointer load above the independent loads, exactly as retail has it** (round 411, battle/main/misc3 func_800785D4, 132 bytes / 33 insns = retail's size with every one of the 33 instructions identical; the residual is only that retail names the flat `g_BattleCommandCodes` / `g_BattleCommandSlotMasks` where the typed `g_BattleCommandRecords[slot].actorSlot / .param` spelling emits the same address as base+addend, and `check_bin.sh battle` prints OK - the MATCH RELOC class). The 75.45% park that had sat there read `pEntry = g_BattleCommandQueue; tile = <two lbu>; store(pEntry);` and its note listed eight spellings; re-measured, that body did not compile at all (it still named two symbols that had since been folded into a struct), and with a pointer local assigned twice cc1 gives BOTH queue pointers `$a2` and keeps the tile `lbu`s below the `sb` (98.79%). Two edits closed it: the tile statement FIRST, and the first store written as `g_BattleCommandRecords[g_BattleCommandQueue[0x2DA]].actorSlot = color;` (the inline form this unit's matched neighbours already use) - that reproduces retail's order AND its register pair (first queue temp `$v0`, the tail pointer `$a2`; with one variable for both, `$a2`/`$a2`). So on a load/store ordering residual: re-measure the park first, then try the inline-global store form with the loading statement first, before believing the note's "gcc cannot do this".
  **cc1 2.7.2 FORWARDS A HALFWORD STORE INTO A LATER `lh` OF THE SAME SYMBOL, SO A RETAIL SEQUENCE `sh $v0,%lo(G)` / ... / `lh $v0,%lo(G)` CANNOT BE WRITTEN BY NAMING G TWICE - and the two escapes both cost the block order** (round 409, world_map/main/misc8 func_80083A00, six switch cases each store a view-rotation halfword and then read it back to scale it by `<< 12`; parked 63.80%, 352 insns against retail's 395). Measured, all six sites the same: naming the symbol (`(s16)g_WorldMapViewRotationY << 12`) folds the read to a constant (`lui $v0,0x40`, 3 insns short per site) even with two pointer stores in between - gcc 2.7.2 does forward across an unknown-base store here, so "the stores defeat it" is false; reading through the neighbouring symbol instead (`((s16 *)&g_WorldMapCameraRot)[1]`, base+addend, the spelling that closed the sibling func_8008390C) stops the fold but cc1 then treats the two symbol_refs as distinct objects and HOISTS the `lh` above the `sh` that writes it (`lh $3,g_WorldMapCameraRot+2` before `sh $2,g_WorldMapCameraRot`), which is a stale-value reorder and silently wrong code; `*(volatile s16 *)&G` keeps the load after the store but every non-volatile store in the case block floats around the barrier (the case's `li`/`sh`/`sw` order scrambles, 59.73%) and the load comes out `lhu` + `sll 16` when the extern is `u16`. So on a diff whose only shape difference is a retail `lh` of a global your body just wrote, the fold is the wall: park with the base+addend spelling (right instruction count) rather than the symbol (right reloc) and say which. Not yet tried from this file's levers: an address-taken pointer local to the global (`h-address-taken-load-blocks-hoist` above) as the load barrier. Round 411 tried it on the world_map/misc7 twin func_80081470: a `s16 *pRot = &g_WorldMapCameraRot;` used for BOTH the -0x80 store and the reload that fills unk58/unk54 does not survive - cc1 propagates the address and emits the store through $at anyway (77.21%, 600 bytes), and a `pRot[0]` read instead of `((s16 *)&g_WorldMapCameraRot)[1]` measures the identical body, so the pointer local is not the lever. In that function the +2 neighbour read was NOT hoisted above the store (retail keeps the store first anyway) but the address is then no longer CSE'd into $a0 the way retail has it, which is the remaining frame difference.
- id: h-stack-arg-store-sink-is-a-war-tie | keywords: store, order, stack, argument, sll, shift, li, schedule, war
  **A 6-ARGUMENT CALL'S SECOND STACK-ARG STORE (`sw v0,0x14(sp)` moved to the END of the argument setup, past `sw ra`) IS NOT A SOURCE ORDER - IT IS THE SHIFT TEMP'S REGISTER: when the index shift of the call's own base pointer takes `$v0` it makes a WAR dependency on the `li v0,0x40` that feeds that store, so sched2 must emit the store before the shift** (round 402, world_map/main/misc11 func_8008B54C and func_8008BDD0, both parked 93.37% at retail's exact 248 bytes / 62 insns, the same 6 lines off). Retail: `li a1,0x110 / sll v1,a0,7 / li v0,0x100 / sw v0,0x10(sp) / li v0,0x40 / ... / sw ra,0x1C(sp) / sw v0,0x14(sp) / jal / addu s0,s0,v1`; ours: `li v0,0x40 / sw v0,0x14(sp) / sll v0,a0,7 / ... / addu s0,s0,v0`. The tail of both functions is already byte-identical, so the whole residual is that allocation. Measured dead on the same body: the tail's source order replaced by the twin's (unk24, dirZ, dirY, dirX, moveSpeed, state, trailDelay, angle) is identical 93.37 (sched2 puts retail's order back), `g_WorldMapSlots + index` in place of `&g_WorldMapSlots[index]` identical, `try_flags.sh --sweep` tops out at 93.37 (2.6.0 93.03, cdk 83.19, -O1 49.74), and a 4-minute permuter run gave PERMUTER_BETTER base 410 best 395 whose only edit swapped SpriteSetScale above SpritePlayAnimation (wrong code). Note that the matched 4-statement-tail twin WorldMapInitPartyActorSprite2 has the SAME retail prologue and does get `sll v1`, so a longer tail is what loses the contest - park this shape rather than hunting a spelling.
  Round 417 confirms the allocno side of it: an explicit `s32 offset = index * 0x80;` with the base built from it (`(WorldMapActor *)((u8 *)g_WorldMapSlots + offset)`) and `g_WorldMapSlots + index` both compile to the SAME body (93.37, 62 insns - cse folds the offset back into the base expression), so the shift's allocno cannot be made to overlap the 0x100/0x40 constant allocnos from source, and `pActor = g_WorldMapSlots; pActor += index;` reads 85.84 because the `lui/lw` of the anim file then moves below the `lui/lw` of g_WorldMapSlots. Both functions re-measured live at 93.37% / retail's 248 bytes before parking.
- id: h-address-taken-load-blocks-hoist | keywords: store, order, hoist, load, address, pointer, local
  **TAKING THE ADDRESS OF THE LOADED FIELD INTO A POINTER LOCAL, AND GROUPING THE REST IN A `do {} while (0)`, STOPS cc1 HOISTING LOADS ABOVE A STORE TO A GLOBAL** (round 378, battling/main/misc4 func_8007A768, 92.44 -> 96.90 at 284 bytes / 71 insns, retail 288). Retail stores `p->y` to one global, loads `p->x` only AFTER that store, `addiu`s the y register by -0x400 and finally stores z, i.e. it never hoists; every plain spelling (`g = p->y; h = p->x; i = p->y - 0x400 - gRig; j = p->z;`, the same with a `s32 y` local, split statements, `*(s32*)&p->x`) hoisted all three p->x/p->y/p->z loads above the first store, because cse will not CSE a load through a parameter across a global store, so the second `p->y` reload came out and the stores were reordered. What reproduced retail's store order and the single load was `y = p->y; gTargetY = y; new_var = &p->x; y -= 0x400; gTarget = *new_var; do { gPosY = y - gRigYOffset; gTargetZ = p->z; } while (0);` - the address-taken pointer is the load barrier (`permute.sh` found it, base 625 -> 70, and its do-while grouping is the h-constant-retail-materialises-head lever above). Residual after it: retail fills the `rand()` jal slot with the `move` of rand's result and uses two registers for x and z where this body reuses one. Reach for it when a diff shows YOUR body loading several struct fields at the top and retail loading each field at its store.
- id: h-forward-defeats-a-struct-store-alias | keywords: store, reload, forward, global, aliasing, halfword, struct, u16
  **READING THE GLOBAL INSTEAD OF A LOCAL DOES NOT BRING BACK A RELOAD RETAIL DOES ACROSS TWO STRUCT-MEMBER STORES: cc1 2.6.0 forwards the stored halfword anyway, because the guard's memory expression survives a store through a struct pointer** (round 410, slus_006.64/system/libarchive func_80028F30, parked 83.12%). Retail stores `sh $v1,%lo(g_ArchiveDebugStreamBlockCount)` from a `lhu` of a sector header, sign-extends that value with `sll 16`/`sra 16` for the `slt` guard, and then RELOADS the symbol with `lh` after two `sh` stores into a `u16` struct (`pSection->state`, `pSection->id`); the parked body held the value in an `s16` local, so the `lh` was missing and the block count came out of the earlier register. Declaring the extern `s16` and writing the guard as `pSection->size < g_ArchiveDebugStreamBlockCount` with the reload as a fresh `nBlockCount = g_ArchiveDebugStreamBlockCount` produced a byte-identical 1300-byte / 325-instruction body (83.12% both ways): gcc 2.6.0 forwards the stored `(mem:HI)` into the later sign-extending load even across the two struct-pointer stores, so the "read the global, not the local" fix does not apply to this shape - check a `MATCH`-less park's missing `lh` against this before spending runs on it.
- id: h-cross-jump-restores-tail | keywords: store, order, hoist, li, cross-jump, do-while, arms, constant
  **A CONSTANT RETAIL MATERIALISES AFTER THE FIRST STORE OF A TAIL SHARED BY TWO ARMS IS `li`-HOISTED TO THE HEAD OF THAT BLOCK BY CC1 UNLESS THE FIRST STORE IS DUPLICATED IN BOTH ARMS, WHICH LETS jump.c CROSS-JUMP IT BACK** (round 396, slus_006.64/system/sound func_800386C4, 97.73 -> 100.00 at retail's exact 352 bytes / 88 insns). Retail ends `if (SoundGetVolumeMode()) { volume <<= 7; ... } else { volume <<= 6; ... }` with a shared tail `sh s0,0x66(s1) / li v0,1 / sh v0,0x36(s1) / sh v0,0x62(s1)`, and every single-copy spelling of that tail emits `li v0,1` FIRST (nested `a = b = 1`, two separate statements, the 0x36/0x62 stores last, a `do {} while (0)` around the pair or around all three, a volatile store on 0x66, and cc1_probe shows cc1 hoists it in a snippet too) - the note's STORE_HOIST class. Writing the FIRST tail store once inside each arm (`pState->unk66 = volume;` as the last statement of both branches, the two flag stores left once below the if/else) makes jump.c merge the identical store + flags into the label retail has at 0x66 and the `li` lands after the store, exactly retail's order; the store order is the whole residual, so nothing else in the body changes. Duplicating all three stores instead gives the right order but moves the `li` into the `beq` delay slot (97.58), so duplicate exactly the one store that retail's jump target sits on - the instruction the label points at is the one the original wrote in both arms.

- id: h-constant-retail-materialises-head | keywords: store, volatile, li, move, sh
  **A constant retail materialises at the HEAD of a store group belongs to the LAST STATEMENT INSIDE the `do {} while (0)` block, not after it** (round 311, probed with cc1_probe then measured in the unit). `do { <six stores>; pActor->command = 1; } while (0);` emits `li $v0,1` at the head of the group and its `sh` after all six stores, which is retail's shape, while `} while (0); pActor->command = 1;` emits the `li` next to its store (92.21 vs 94.71 and 97.97 on world_map/misc6's twin pair). This block is the family's only ordering lever, so when a park's residual is a constant in the wrong register, move the statement INSIDE the block before trying anything else.
- id: h-store-read-back-pair | keywords: store, volatile, sh, lh, lhu, sll
  **For a store-then-read-it-back pair, only the STORE may be volatile** (round 312, `cc1_probe.sh` then measured in the unit): `*(volatile s16 *)pAngle = 0x10;` followed by a plain `pAngle[0] << 12` gives retail's `sh` then `lh` then `sll 12`; a volatile READ gives `lhu` plus sll16/sra4 and a plain store folds the read to a constant. Make the first N-1 of a group volatile, never the read, and re-measure after each one.
- id: h-uninitialised-pointer-assignment-places-the-load | keywords: store, order, hoist, load, pointer, local, initialiser, placement
  **A POINTER LOCAL DECLARED WITHOUT AN INITIALISER AND ASSIGNED AT THE LINE WHERE RETAIL'S SYMBOL LOAD SITS PUTS THAT LOAD IN RETAIL'S SLOT; THE SAME LOAD AS A DECLARATION INITIALISER LANDS IN THE PROLOGUE** (round 398, world_map/main/misc7 func_800817A0, 79.06 -> 89.56 at retail's exact 200 bytes, residual is one hoisted load elsewhere in the block). Retail loads `lw s1,%lo(g_WorldMapGroups)` between the `g_WorldMapPlayerPositionY` load and its `addu`, i.e. mid-body; `WorldMapEntry *pEntry = &g_WorldMapGroups[2];` at the top of the function emits that load plus the `sw s1,0x14(sp)` save in the prologue, and `WorldMapEntry *pEntry;` ... `pEntry = &g_WorldMapGroups[2];` placed between the `pSlot->pos.y` and `pSlot->pos.z` lines reproduces retail's position exactly and is worth the 10 points. The same body's other residue shows the barrier is NOT general: a `do { } while (0)` inserted just before the following store scores 86.48 and a scratch local loaded at that point 89.56 (no change), so use the uninitialised-assignment form, not an empty block, when the diff shows your symbol load in the prologue and retail's mid-body.
- id: h-global-assigned-early-body | keywords: store, volatile, loop, move, prologue
  **A global assigned EARLY in the body keeps its load BEFORE the calls.** round 313 `battling/misc5` func_8007DC74: `pSlot = (BattleSpriteSlot *)g_BattleSpriteSlots;` placed between two field assignments that precede the calls is worth 76.42 -> 78.83 and retail's exact byte count; moving the same assignment down to the loop makes gcc defer the load past the calls and the whole prologue is one register off. So when a park is exactly one saved register away, move the global's assignment UP, not the loop's body around.
- id: h-retail-s-repeated-reload | keywords: store, volatile, pointer
  **Retail's repeated reload of a global means the source re-read it at every site**: keep `g_BattleTargetSlotEntry->field` / `g_BattleCurrentRow->field` INLINE in each statement rather than hoisting them into a pointer local (round 313, `battle/misc18`, required for 100.00).
- id: h-volatile-store-fix-load | keywords: store, volatile, sched2, hoist, lhu, li, sh, u16
  **A `volatile` STORE is the fix for a load that sched2 raises above it** (round 316, two units): `*(volatile u16*)&pHeader->state = 1;` moved `archive` func_8002B8B0 91.85 -> 97.95 because a plain store lets sched2 hoist the following `lhu` above the `li/sh` pair and hand the constant the wrong register. The pair is: volatile STORE, never the read (see the store-then-read-back lever).
- id: h-volatile-store-lvalue-first | keywords: store, volatile, sb, sw, delay, jal, reorg
  **A volatile STORE lvalue on the FIRST N-1 members of a store group fixes the group's ORDER.** round 321: `*(volatile u32 *)&p->color.word = ...` on the first two of three colour stores is what produced retail's `sb/sw/sb/sw/sb/sw(jal slot)` and put the constants in $a0/$a2; without it gcc sinks both `sw`s past the following `sb` and picks $a2/$a3. All three volatile is 99.00 - the LAST store must stay plain so reorg can steal it into the following call's delay slot - and a `do {} while (0)` around the same block is worse. This is the store-order sibling of the volatile-store-before-a-load lever.
- id: h-volatile-read-brings-back | keywords: store, volatile, cc1, pointer, switch, u16
  **A `volatile` READ brings back a load retail performs twice, which this cc1 folds into one** (round 330): `func_8008848C` reads the script instruction pointer once for the command byte and once for the step; 2.7.2 folds them, which is why the body was recorded as gcc-2.6.0-only. `*(volatile u16 *)&g_FieldScriptVMCurActor->scriptInstructionPointer += 2;` restored the second load and the body matched at 100.00 under the unit's OWN preset - no split. So before accepting a "this body is another compiler" verdict, check whether the delta is a fold you can disable with a volatile read; that is cheaper than a unit switch and it does not touch gears.toml.
- id: h-volatile-read-costs-a-mask | keywords: store, volatile, cse, lhu, andi, reload, global, u16
  **A `volatile` READ RESTORES RETAIL'S SECOND LOAD OF A GLOBAL, BUT EVERY LATER READ OF THAT SAME DECLARATION IN THE FUNCTION THEN COMES OUT `lhu` + `andi 0xffff` WHERE RETAIL HAS A BARE `lhu`** (round 406, battle/main/misc1 func_8007252C, parked 93.02% at 904 bytes / 226 insns against retail's 908 / 227). Retail reloads `g_BattleStateFlags` three times in the tail (once per `& 0x7F8` test, once per `& 0x7` test, once for the mask local) while non-volatile C cse's them into one load - 892 bytes, 91.63%. `extern volatile u16 g_BattleStateFlags;` brings both test reloads back and is worth +12 bytes and +1.4 points, but the mask read then emits `lui v0 / lhu v0 / andi s2,v0,0xffff / move a0,s2` where retail has `lui s2 / lhu s2 / move a0,s2` (cc1 masks a volatile HImode load's high bits), so that one read has to be spelled non-volatile (`*(u16 *)&g_BattleStateFlags`) for the tail to be exact. Only mark the reads that retail reloads; a volatile declaration is per-TU, so check every other user of the global in the unit before committing it.
- id: h-volatile-store-one-field | keywords: store, volatile, cse, lhu, sll, sra, struct
  A VOLATILE STORE ON ONE FIELD beats a folded write-back: when retail stores a computed field and RELOADS it in the next statement and yours folds the arithmetic away (startX = a*64 then deltaX = (startX - d*64)/divisor folding to (a - d)*64), make that one store a volatile one: *(volatile s16 *)&pCmd->startX = a * 64; - it invalidates the memory CSE entry and the reload comes back (battle/misc22 func_800A32D8, 74.21 -> 100.00). volatile on the STRUCT FIELDS is the wrong lever (gcc then loads them lhu + sll/sra).
- id: h-do-while-block-lever | keywords: store, volatile, cc1, move
  THE DO/WHILE-BLOCK LEVER: when a 85%+ body differs only in the ORDER of two adjacent store clusters, wrap the smaller cluster in 'do { ... } while (0);' - cc1 then schedules it as a unit and the cluster can move across unrelated stores. It closed world_map/misc4 (70.00 -> 100, four stores) and battle/misc13 (92.28 -> 100, one call) in round 72. A bare '{ }' block, or a block narrowed to two statements, does NOT work; keep every statement inside it and leave a one-line comment, because it looks gratuitous and is not.
- id: h-store-whose-value-global | keywords: store, volatile, lw, sh, cast, hoist, u8
  A STORE WHOSE VALUE IS A GLOBAL CANNOT BE TYPED when retail interleaves load/store ('lw PosX / sh 0x14 / lw PosY / sh 0x16 / lw PosZ / sh 0x18'): every typed spelling ('pEffect->x = g_BattleEffectPosX', an SVECTOR view, a s16* view, a volatile read) makes gcc hoist ALL the loads above the store group and scores 63.89-70.80% at the same size, in three units so far (battling/misc2, world_map/misc5, system/system). The load-bearing shape is an 's16' store through an address built from a 'u8 *' cast; keep it and report ROUGH with the number. Typing the ROTATION stores of the same record still matches, so do not conclude "field typing is bad" - it is only the global-sourced stores.
- id: h-five-shapes-four-round | keywords: store, volatile, hoist, array, cast, cc1, pointer, s32
  FIVE SHAPES, not four (round 299 added STORE_HOIST): **STORE_HOIST** - retail computes each store's value inside that store's block and cc1 hoists the later values to the top of the basic block. This one is PROVEN unreachable from source: a two-store replica under cc1_probe.sh keeps the hoist with inline values, an embedded assignment, a brace block per store, per-store do{}while(0), per-store scoped temporaries, an s16 destination cast, array vs pointer addressing and plain stores, under gcc 2.6.0, 2.7.2 AND 2.7.2-cdk (-O1 removes it but rewrites the body, 77 percent). Two positive datapoints if you inherit one: giving every value its OWN local assigned at its first store reproduces retail's register assignment exactly (battle/misc16 94.88), and ESCAPING THE ARGUMENT'S ADDRESS (s32 *p = &arg;) defeats the hoist.
- id: h-stack-arg-u16-load-hoist | keywords: hoist, stack, u16, lhu, frame, cse, arg
  **SOLVED in round 402: the load is not hoisted by the scheduler, it is emitted in the function PROLOGUE by assign_parms for any narrow stack parameter - declare the parameter as a 4-byte AGGREGATE and the load moves to its use (see `j-a-narrow-stack-argument-is`). The paragraph below is the pre-402 state of the search, kept for its measurements.** battle/misc16 func_8008F0A8 (round 389): retail has `lhu a0,0x30(sp)` in the load-delay slot of the y2 store's index `lw` plus `addiu a0,a0,-16` after the address sum, our body emits the same two instructions at the top of the block (right after the slot-graphics `lw s1`) - 4 displaced lines, 80.42% at retail's exact 1596 bytes / 399 insns. `u16 *pHeight = &arg4;` with `(*pHeight - 16) / 2` reproduces retail's EXACT instruction stream (98.47%) but gcc homes an address-taken stack parameter: +`lhu v0,0x38(sp)` +`sh v0,0x10(sp)`, frame 0x20 -> 0x28, 1604 bytes / 401 insns, so it can never link. `const u16 arg4` homes identically, `volatile u16 arg4` is worse (96.61). So the `s32 *p = &arg;` escape that defeats STORE_HOIST only works for a REGISTER-passed argument (a0-a3); a stack argument pays an 8-byte frame and 2 instructions. Displacing one statement (`s32 half = (arg4 - 16) / 2;` at the store) does not move the load either (80.64, 1592): only the pure load hoists, the arithmetic stays with the statement.
- id: h-store-must-stay-ahead | keywords: store, volatile, hoist
  A STORE THAT MUST STAY AHEAD OF A FOLLOWING VOLATILE RELOAD OF THE SAME BASE needs the store's lvalue made volatile through the base type: *(ActorData* volatile*)&g_FieldActors[i].pActorData = HeapAlloc(0x138, 0); - the plain store lets gcc hoist the second base load above it (field/misc8 func_80080F44, 90.26 -> 96.36 at retail's exact size).
- id: h-volatile-cse-lever-has | keywords: store, volatile, cse, loop, lui, lw, u16, u8
  THE VOLATILE CSE LEVER HAS TWO DIRECTIONS, and both are worth trying before any register reasoning (rounds 299-300 closed two parks and one function with them). (a) READ: *(volatile u16 *)&g_Sym (or u32/u8) INSIDE the loop/body brings back a load retail has and you do not - battle/misc15 func_8008ADD0 went from one instruction short of link size to 99.90 at retail's exact 824 bytes on that alone.
