(b) WRITE: a store through a volatile lvalue invalidates the memory-CSE entry, so retail's RELOAD after the store comes back - battling/misc3 func_80077038 needed BOTH of two |= stores made volatile (*(volatile u32 *)&p->flags |= 0x1000; p->vy -= 0x50; *(volatile u32 *)&p->flags |= 0x400;) to reproduce retail's three lw 0xD0 reloads and an un-hoisted lui 0x800 (100.00, 1356 bytes). A battle/misc22 volatile s16 find and a world_map/misc6 six-store do{}while(0) find are the same mechanism from other angles.
- id: h-plain-load-between-store-readback | keywords: store, volatile, lh, cse, read-back, order, sh, mode
  **A PLAIN GLOBAL LOAD BETWEEN A STORE AND ITS READ-BACK brings retail's `sh`/`lh` reload back without any volatile** (round 389, world_map/misc11 func_8008BB40, park 87.64 -> 99.92 with the table operand as the only residual, check_bin OK). Retail does `sh $v1,0x48($s0)` then `lh $v1,0x48($s0)` for `drawAngle = angle`; that looked like a volatile-store case (the previous round's volatile u16 attempt scored 84.34 and lost the rest of the block), but the real source order was `pActor->angle = g_GameState.worldMapPlayerRotation; mode = g_WorldMapMode; pActor->moveSpeed = ...; pActor->trailDelay = ...; pActor->drawAngle = pActor->angle; switch (mode)` - exactly the already-matched twin WorldMapModeInitActorSlot's body, whose own code does the same reload at 100.00. Porting that statement order ALSO fixed the block's load order (zeros, lhu angle, lw mode, then the constant stores). So in the WorldMap actor-init family: port the twin's statement order and its `*(volatile s32 *)&pActor->x = g_WorldMapPlayerPosition;` store BEFORE reaching for volatile, and give `mode` its own local so the switch reads the load retail hoists.
- id: h-global-declared-type-volatile | keywords: store, volatile, pointer, s32, lui, lw
  A global declared 'Type * volatile' (here g_FieldActors) reloads its pointer for EVERY store written inline. Reassign a pointer local once per pair of stores - 'pActor = (Type*)(offset + (s32)g_FieldActors);' then both stores through it - and keep the byte offset in one s32 local so gcc does not re-form 'index * sizeof(...)' (field/misc6, round 37: 6 lui/lw against retail's 3).

- id: h-script-byte-shifted-one | keywords: store, hoist, lbu, sll, local, script
  **A script byte shifted for ONE store must reach that store through a LOCAL.** round 389 `slus_006.64/system/sound` func_8003DC50 (port of its twin SoundScriptSetTriangleEnvelope2): with `delayReload = pScript[2] << 2;` assigned before the store group and the field storing `delayReload`, retail's shape comes out at 100.00; inlining `pAudioElements->envelopes[1].delayReload = pScript[2] << 2;` makes cc1 hoist the `lbu`/`sll` up into the middle of the group and the body drops to 91.68 with the mask `lhu`/`ori`/`sh` and the delay store swapping registers. Same family as STORE_HOIST, positive direction: one local per value, assigned at its source position.

- id: h-constant-store-group-hoist | keywords: store, hoist, li, la, cc1, zero, probe, order
  **cc1 HOISTS A CONSTANT STORE GROUP ABOVE A RUN OF ZERO STORES WHEN THE BASIC BLOCK ENDS WITH AN `la` STATEMENT, AND HOISTS ONLY THE `li` WHEN IT DOES NOT** (round 391, world_map/main/misc1 func_80072238, park 99.81 at retail's exact 1892 bytes, measured with cc1_probe.sh under the unit's own flags). Retail emits `li v1,1` above `li v0,2` and its two `sw v1` AFTER five `sw $0` stores. Written in retail's store order (ones after the zeros) our body emits `li v0,1` + both stores ABOVE the zeros; 11 source orders all land in 98.7-99.8, so the statement order is not the lever. The probe isolates it: five zero stores followed by two `= 1` stores and then a third statement storing a function address gives `li 1 / sw / sw` BEFORE the zeros; the same block with only TWO zero stores, or with the address statement anywhere but last, gives `li 1` early and the stores in place (retail's shape). So for this shape, do not permute the stores: change what ends the block (a trailing statement of a different kind, or a `do {} while (0)` around the store group per h-do-while-block-lever) and re-probe before parking.
- id: h-no-flags-local-for-a-masked-store | keywords: store, flags, mask, local, and, lw, slot, read-modify-write
  **A TAIL'S MASKED FLAG WORD IS `p->flags &= ~MASK;` IN PLACE, NOT A `flags` LOCAL - the local makes cc1 hoist the `and` two slots early** (round 397, field/main/misc12 func_8009AEE0, 92.39 -> 93.65 at retail's exact 636 bytes / 159 insns). Retail's arrival tail is `sh x2 rotation / lui+ori mask / move v0,zero / lw a0,0(s2) / two 16.16 position stores / sh 0 stuckFrameCount / and a0,a0,a1 / j / sw a0,0(s2)` - i.e. the load and the mask constant sit at the head of the group and only the `and` travels to the end. Reading the word into a `flags` local and storing `flags & ~MASK` at the end (which an earlier park note recommended) puts `lw` and `and` adjacent at the head and costs 8 bytes' worth of displacement in a `-fschedule-insns` unit; the in-place `&=` spelling reproduces both retail slots. So for a read-modify-write flag store at the end of a block, measure BOTH spellings before keeping the local - the local is only the answer when retail's `and` sits next to its load.

- id: h-no-way-to-home-a | keywords: constant, li, hoist, top, pair, duplicate, store, park
  **A CONSTANT WHOSE TWO/THREE USES ARE LATE IN THE BODY CANNOT BE `li`-ED AT INSN 1 WITHOUT ALSO EMITTING ITS FIRST STORE THERE; this shape has TWO parks and no known source** (round 397, battle/main/misc24 func_800AA898, parked 97.44% / 160 bytes vs retail 156, and its identical twin lib_gear/main func_801E3534, parked 94.87% since round 370). Retail is 39 insns starting `addiu v1,zero,-1` whose three uses are `sh v1` at 0x98, 0x3A and 0x1E (the last in the `jr` delay slot). All three -1 stores in their logical places: 39 insns / 156 bytes / 74.36%, `li` at slot 7 whatever the spelling (`s16 m1 = -1;` with three uses, `m1 = -1;`, `arg1 = -1;`, a dead local, literals) because cse substitutes a constant for its register and rematerialises it at the first store's `force_reg`. A DUPLICATE early store of the same value (any of the three fields): 97.44%, `li` at slot 1 as retail, and the surplus `sh` lands at slot 9 - gcc 2.6 never deletes it (try_flags --sweep: 2.6.0/2.7.2/2.7.2-cdk x -O1/O2/O3 x -G4/G8 x mcpu3000/r8000 all <= 97.44, so not a preset). Moving the single store to the top: 94.87, `li` at 1 but the store at 9 instead of 15. A `do {} while (0)` around the whole store group changes nothing (74.36, `li` still 7). So neither the store-hoist nor the block-end lever moves this one: park on it.
Round 428 added the probe that closes the last spelling door on the same function: DROP the leading constant triplet (`sh 0xFFFF` at 0x3C, `sb 0xFF` at 0x5C, `sb 0x6B` at 0x39) and the SAME body hoists both constant homes to slots 0 and 1 (`li v1,-1 / li v0,1 / sw a2,8 ...`), so the triplet is what displaces the -1 home by five slots to sit between `li v0,0x6B` and its `sb`; retail has the identical triplet and its -1 home at slot 0, i.e. retail's home was created ahead of the triplet by something that emits no instruction. Four more no-instruction uses of -1 measured with cc1_probe.sh on battle/main/misc24's flags and all still at slot 5: `arg1 = -1;` on the unused parameter, a dead `if (n != -1)` branch that fold deletes, `s16 n = -1;` reached by all three stores, and `*(s32 *)(arg0+0x4) = n == -1;`. The honest body (no duplicate) is retail's EXACT 156 bytes / 39 insns with one displaced instruction, so its 74.36 is objdiff non-monotonicity and not a distance - quote 156/39 with it.

- id: h-all-volatile-pins-straight-line | keywords: store, volatile, order, hoist, schedule, straight-line, delay, slot, permuter
  **WHEN RETAIL IS A PURE STRAIGHT-LINE STORE/LOAD CLUSTER IN SOURCE ORDER AND EVERY STATEMENT REORDERING COMPILES TO THE SAME REARRANGED BYTES, PIN THE WHOLE CLUSTER WITH VOLATILE CASTS AND LET THE PERMUTER PLACE THE LAST TWO INSTRUCTIONS** (round 399, world_map/main/misc5 func_8007C724, park 44.62 -> 100.00 at retail's exact 180 bytes / 45 insns; same class as misc4 WorldMapResetActorSlotWithSound 70% and misc6 func_80080578 34.97%). Measured: three genuinely different statement orders (locals first, loads embedded at the stores, retail's own output order) all compile to the SAME schedule - gcc 2.7.2 -O2 canonicalises the block, so statement order is provably not the lever; -fno-schedule-insns/-fno-schedule-insns2/both score 49-56 (and break every matched neighbour), -O1 70.09 (breaks the unit), 2.6.0/cdk worse. Making EVERY global and pPlayer store volatile (loads too, values cached in plain locals) reproduces retail's order wholesale; the tail then needs two more edits the permuter found in 59 s: (1) the cameraRot store through a pointer local assigned mid-cluster (`s16 *pRot; pRot = &g_WorldMapCameraRot; ... *(volatile s16 *)pRot = -0x40;`), and (2) an EMPTY `do { } while (0);` right before that store, whose basic-block boundary is what puts the plain (non-volatile) last store in the `jr` delay slot instead of the return `li 1`. The last store itself must stay NON-volatile (reorg never moves a volatile store into a slot) and the second-to-last volatile. Check the two halves separately: unpinned tail gives the return `li` in the slot, all-volatile tail leaves an unfilled `nop` (4 bytes over).
- id: h-volatile-cluster-needs-its-global-load-first | keywords: store, volatile, order, hoist, straight-line, load, delay, nop
  **ON A VOLATILE-PINNED STORE CLUSTER, THE GLOBAL LOAD RETAIL HAS AT THE HEAD OF THE CLUSTER MUST BE ITS OWN STATEMENT ABOVE THE FIRST VOLATILE STORE - a volatile store is a barrier, so cc1 can no longer hoist a non-volatile load past it and the load instead lands at its own statement with an unfilled load-delay `nop` (4 bytes over)** (round 412, world_map/main/misc6 func_8007ECA4, park 78.97 -> 95.00 (404 bytes, 101 insns) with only the volatile casts, then 100.00 at retail's exact 400 bytes / 100 insns once `nIndex = g_WorldMapAreaIndex;` moved above the nine-store cluster). Retail's tail is `lw g_WorldMapAreaIndex` / nine `li`+`sw`/`sh` stores / the x,y,z computations, i.e. source order, and three plain spellings all canonicalised differently (78.48 with a pointer local for the group, 78.97 once the tail re-read `g_WorldMapGroups`); `*(volatile s32 *)&pActor->dirX = ...` on every store reproduced retail's order in one edit. Same mechanism as h-all-volatile-pins-straight-line, with the extra rule for the leading global load. The last store (z) can stay volatile here because retail's `jr` slot is the `nop` it leaves, not an instruction.
- id: h-single-decl-makes-a-copy | keywords: store, order, hoist, copy, alias, struct, decl, interleave
  **A RUN OF WORD COPIES BETWEEN TWO INDICES OF ONE TABLE ONLY KEEPS RETAIL'S INTERLEAVED `lw`/`sw` (ONE TEMP, EACH LOAD JUST BEFORE ITS STORE) WHEN ALL OF THEM GO THROUGH ONE DECLARATION; FOUR SEPARATE SYMBOL DECLARATIONS LET cc1 PROVE THEY DO NOT ALIAS AND IT HOISTS ALL THE LOADS** (round 399, battle/main/misc4 func_80078E24, 99.14% at retail's exact 140 insns / 560 bytes, `check_bin.sh battle` OK - the residual is only the folded reloc names, the original object names D_800D3404/D_800D3408/D_800D340C). The body copies the first four words of `g_BattleSlotRegs[slot - 3]` to `g_BattleSlotRegs[arg0 - 3]`; spelled through the four symbols the retail object actually relocs it is 76-78% / 572 bytes with four `lw` above four `sw` and four live temps, and spelling all four through the one array with a byte offset in a `s32` local (`*(u32 *)((u8 *)g_BattleSlotRegs + dst + 0x4) = *(u32 *)((u8 *)g_BattleSlotRegs + src + 0x4);`) reproduces retail exactly, because variable indices into the same decl are may-alias and the loads cannot move. Two companions measured on the same body: the typed array form (`g_BattleSlotRegs[arg0 - MAX_PARTY_MEMBERS].field`, local or inline) folds the `- 3` element bias into the ADDRESS CONSTANT (`lui/addiu base-0xC0` plus an `addu`) and costs 2 instructions (86.14 with index locals), so keep the byte offset in a local and let every statement name the base symbol; and declaring/assigning `dst` BEFORE `src` swapped the two index registers `$a1`/`$a2` AND put the `memmove` size (`li a2,0x170`) into the `jal` delay slot - the last 9% of that function was that declaration order.
- id: h-one-decl-for-a-record | keywords: store, reload, queue, pointer, record, field, count, decl
  **THREE FIELD STORES THROUGH `records[queue->count].f` REPRODUCE RETAIL'S PARTIAL QUEUE-POINTER RELOADS; SPELLED AS THREE SEPARATE ARRAY SYMBOLS THEY RELOAD ON EVERY STORE** (same round 399 function, and its matched neighbour BattleQueueCommandF8 in the same unit is the template). Retail loads `g_BattleCommandQueue` once and uses it for the first two stores, then reloads it for the third and again for `count++`; the single struct-array spelling (`g_BattleCommandRecords[g_BattleCommandQueue->count].unk24/.command/.actorSlot`) gives exactly that (140 vs 140 insns), while `*(u16 *)(g_BattleCommandSlotMasks + count * 72)`, `g_BattleCommandSlotIds[count * 72]` and `g_BattleCommandCodes[count * 72]` - three parallel arrays, one per field - add 3 instructions and score 78.50. The three names are fields +0x24/+0x31/+0x0D of the 0x48-byte record at g_BattleCommandRecords, so the typed form is the realistic one too, and the folded relocs are the only objdiff difference.
- id: h-no-adjacent-store-merge | keywords: store, merge, adjacent, wide, widths, sb, sh, sw, probe, park
  **NO AVAILABLE COMPILER MERGES TWO ADJACENT CONSTANT FIELD STORES INTO ONE WIDER STORE, so a retail body whose only residual is `sh 0x6010` where yours emits `sb 0x10 / sb 0x60` is unreachable from source - and the merge widths can be internally inconsistent, which rules out a wider struct type as the explanation** (round 399, battling/main/misc8 func_80081ECC, parked 70.16% at 752 bytes vs retail 684, 188 insns vs 171). Retail builds a POLY_G4 with `sh 0x6010` for (r1,g1), `sh 0x7F7F` for (r2,g2)/(r3,g3), `sw 0x140` for (x1,y1), `sw 0` for (x0,y0), `sw 0x600000` for (x2,y2), `sw 0x600140` for (x3,y3), and the SPRT template with `sw 0x64707070` for (r0,g0,b0,code), `sw 0` for (x0,y0), `sh 0` for (u0,v0) and `sw 0xDB0080` for (w,h) - 13 of the 17 extra instructions. Probed with `cc1_probe.sh` on `p->r1 = 0x10; p->g1 = 0x60; p->x1 = 0x140; p->y1 = 0;` (POLY_G4*, and a globals-only variant): gcc-2.7.2-psx, gcc-2.6.0-psx and gcc-2.7.2-cdk-psx each emit sb/sb and sh/sh, never a merged store, so no preset reaches it. It cannot be the source's typing either: in the SAME struct r0,g0 stay two `sb` while r1,g1 merge, so no field type explains both. The merge also cascades - `setSemiTrans` on the merged code byte becomes a real `lbu/andi 0xFE/ori 2` RMW in retail, while the byte-store spelling constant-folds ours to `li 0x66`. Before parking this shape, spend the probe on ONE pair to confirm the compiler cannot merge, then write the park note with the width list.
- id: h-two-volatile-casts-force-a-reload-and-the-store-order | keywords: store, volatile, reload, cse, register, order, sched2, statement
  **A GLOBAL THAT RETAIL RE-READS AFTER A STORE TO A DIFFERENT GLOBAL IS UNREACHABLE FROM A PLAIN READ - gcc 2.7.2 keeps the value in a REGISTER across stores (a store does not kill a register) - so the re-read has to be its own statement through a volatile cast, and the store that must stay ABOVE it has to be volatile too or sched2 sinks it below the load** (round 420, battling/main/misc8 func_80081A44: 732 bytes / 97.74 -> 744 bytes = retail's exact size at 99.70, and `culprit.sh battling` then reports only 3 bytes in one register). Retail case 2 loads g_BattleSlotConfirmFlags, stores the first `nextListIndex`, RELOADS the flags (`lui v0`/`lw v0`/`nop`), stores the second, and reloads a third time for its `== 3` test. `flags = *(volatile s32 *)&g_BattleSlotConfirmFlags;` as a statement between the two stores gives exactly that reload; the same cast inline in the ternary does not (`andi v0,v1,` reuse, plus it lets the allocator hoist the shared constant 1 into `$s1`, +2 insns of frame). Making the FIRST store volatile as well (`*(volatile u16 *)&D_800916E2 = ...`) is what pins retail's store-then-load order - with a plain store the body is 740 bytes and the store lands after the load. Two companions: a call argument that retail forms as `addiu a0,s0,0x3C` off a base it already holds must be written `p + 1` off that base, not as a subscript of the array (`&g_BattleTextLists[6]` costs a lui+addiu); and a store whose target has a relocation ADDEND (`g_BattleTextLists[5].nextListIndex`) makes cc1 materialise the address in a register (3 insns) where the flat `D_800916E2` name gives retail's `lui at` + `sh` (2) - the typed spelling costs a byte here, unlike the folded-reloc cases.
- id: h-store-into-a-local-forces | keywords: store, reload, cse, alias, sb, lhu, dead, spill, array
  **A STORE INTO A 3-BYTE LOCAL IS WHAT FORCES RETAIL'S RE-READS OF A CONSTANT LOAD: gcc 2.7.2 has no alias analysis, so each `sb` invalidates the CSE'd `lhu`, and a run of `sb 0x0/0x1/0x2(sp)` that looks like a dead register spill is really `u8 rgb[3]`/a 3-u8 local struct read back in the same expression** (round 400, world_map/main/misc13 func_800931D8, parked 69.59%). Retail's CLUT fade loads `*pIn` at the inner-loop head and then re-reads it with `lhu v0,0(a3)` three more times, once after each byte store, and never loads the local back (`lbu 0(sp)` appears nowhere) because the local's value is still in a register at each read. Spelled as three `u8` scalars the body loses all three re-reads; spelled as `u8 rgb[3]` the three `sb`s appear AND the frame becomes retail's 0x28 with five callee-saved saves (61.69% measured, 360 vs retail's 380 bytes) - the remaining residual in that function is elsewhere (the three `fr*t` mults, which loop.c hoists for every spelling tried; see g-construct-decides-loop-c before retrying). So when a park note says "dead stores" or "spills", check whether the stored values are read back through a register: the dead store may be the whole point.

- id: h-one-local-per-repeated-read | keywords: store, reload, local, twice, pair, aliasing, stack, array, cse
  **A FIELD READ TWICE FOR TWO ADJACENT STORES MUST GO THROUGH ONE LOCAL; with the expression inline the second read comes back, because the store between them is through a base gcc cannot prove disjoint from the array.** Measured in field/main/misc6 func_8009E574 (parked 79.89 -> 80.25 at 656 bytes against retail's 664). Retail reads `points[walkmeshId].vy` ONCE (`lh v1,2(v1)`) and stores it to both `transformMatrix.t[1]` and `childMatrix.t[1]`, i.e. `sw v1,0x24(v0)` + `sw v1,0x44(v0)`; the inline pair `pFieldActor->transformMatrix.t[1] = points[pActor->walkmeshId].vy; pFieldActor->childMatrix.t[1] = ...;` emits the whole walkmeshId chain twice (`lh`/`sll`/`addu`/`lh`) and costs 7 instructions, because `points` is a stack array whose address escaped to a call and `pFieldActor` comes from an extern pointer, so both are in alias set 0. `s16 nWalkY;` assigned between the two statements reproduces retail. Opposite direction to h-script-byte-shifted-one: this one is about a SECOND READ of the same lvalue, not a shifted value.
- id: h-index-global-re-read-per | keywords: volatile, index, reload, chain, cse, global, stride
  **AN INDEX GLOBAL RETAIL RE-READS FOR EVERY STATEMENT IN A BLOCK MUST BE DECLARED `volatile` IN THAT UNIT, or cse keeps one `index * stride` chain in a register for the whole block and the body is dozens of instructions short** (round 400, field/main/misc2 func_80077268, 68.46 -> 80.73, 135 -> 174 insns of retail's 183, on that one declaration). Retail's twelve in-loop copy statements each start `lui v1,%hi(g_PlayerActorIndex) / lw v1 / nop` and then re-form the whole `sll/addu/sll/subu` chain; `g_FieldActors` was already `FieldActor* volatile`, which brings back the per-statement BASE load but not the index chain, because gcc does not invalidate a non-volatile load's CSE entry across a volatile load. Declaring `extern volatile s32 g_PlayerActorIndex;` in the unit (it is plain `s32` everywhere else in the overlay - a per-TU qualifier is legal and invisible in the object) restores every chain. Symptom to look for: your loop body has ONE `lw <index>` where retail has one per statement, and your instruction count is short by ~4 per statement.
- id: h-volatile-destination-store-pins | keywords: volatile, store, sched2, hoist, load, delay, nop, slot
  **A `volatile` DESTINATION STORE PINS THE STATEMENT'S LOAD/STORE SLOT AND STOPS sched2 FROM SINKING THE NEXT STATEMENT'S VOLATILE LOAD INTO IT** (round 400, field/main/misc2 func_80077268, 86.28 -> 97.43, 173 -> 183 instructions at retail's exact 732 bytes, ten instructions). Six field-to-field copies of the shape `pDst->position.x = ((SpriteData*)g_FieldActors[g_PlayerActorIndex].pSpriteData)->position.x;` with plain stores let sched2 hoist the NEXT statement's `lw g_PlayerActorIndex` between this statement's value load (`lw v0,4(v0)` / `lw v0,0(v0)`) and its `sw`, killing the two load-delay `nop`s retail has. Writing each destination through a volatile lvalue - `*(volatile int*)&pSpriteData->position.x = ...` - blocks that reorder and reproduces retail's `lw/nop/lw/nop/sw` group; the SOURCE side needs nothing (it is already a volatile load). Same mechanism as `h-volatile-store-fix-load` (store, not read) but the other direction: there the store pins a later global load, here it pins the statement's own load.

- id: h-volatile-cast-defeats-the-giv | keywords: store, volatile, base, giv, offset, delay-slot, reorg, tie
  **A `volatile` CAST ON A FIELD STORE COSTS RETAIL'S GIV BASE: where a loop's offsetable giv (`base + constant`) is what lets retail encode a store as `<const-2>(givreg)`, every volatile spelling of that store forms the address off the plain pointer instead, while the plain field store gets the giv base and then has the store stolen into a branch delay slot** (round 404, slus_006.64/system/sound func_8003C4C4, parked 99.96% at retail's exact 548 bytes / 137 insns, ONE instruction left). Retail writes the element's second flag halfword as `sh a3,2(a0)` where `$a0 = $a1 + 2` is the loop's second induction variable (`&status_flags`, the element base +2 that the whole body reaches its fields through, so every other access reads `0xNN-2(a0)`). Plain `pElement->rampFlags = elementFlags;` DOES reproduce that encoding - the giv absorbs the +4 address as giv+2 - but reorg then moves the store into the `bne` delay slot and pushes the following decrement out of it (98.10, 8 lines off, same bytes). `*(volatile u16*)(&pElement->status_flags + 1)`, `*(volatile u16*)&pElement->rampFlags` and `((volatile AudioElement*)pElement)->rampFlags` all keep retail's position and emit `sh a3,4(a1)` off the element base (99.96, one line) because the cast creates a new address pseudo instead of reusing the giv. A `u16 *pFlags = &pElement->status_flags` local avoids neither: it settles in a fresh register (`$t5`) and costs 2-4 lines (99.53 with one store through it, 99.09 with two). So when retail's base register sits a CONSTANT offset away from the pointer your C has, the thing being asked for is the loop's offsetable giv (cc1 creates one per loop, for one constant), and a volatile cast, a pointer local or a split address all destroy it. 3-minute permuter run (1417 iterations, base 10) found nothing.
- id: h-named-half-of-a-split-shift | keywords: sll, sra, shift, order, store, named, intermediate, hoist
  **WHEN RETAIL'S `sll` AND `sra` OF THE SAME VALUE STRADDLE A STORE, THE SOURCE KEPT THE INTERMEDIATE `<< N` IN A NAMED VALUE AND APPLIED `>> N` AT THE USE** (round 404, battling/main/misc2 func_80074BA4, 99.08 -> 99.74 at retail's exact 1212 bytes / 303 insns; the shift pair was the whole of the first residual). Retail is `addu v0,v1,v0 / sll v1,v1,16 / sh v0,0x99E(s0) / sll v0,v0,16 / sra v0,v0,20 / sra v1,v1,20 / lhu a0,0x12(s2) / subu s1,v0,v1`, i.e. `old`'s `sll` is emitted BEFORE the store of the new accumulator and its `sra` only after the new value's own `sra`. The one-expression spelling `delta = ((p0->frameAccum << 16) >> 20) - ((old << 16) >> 20);` emits BOTH of old's shifts after the store (99.08), and hoisting the complete conversion (`oldFrames = (old << 16) >> 20;` above the store) puts the pair adjacently before it (99.04, worse). Splitting the shift itself reproduces retail exactly: `oldFrames = old << 16;` before the store and `- (oldFrames >> 20)` in the delta expression - the `sll` lands early because the named value is defined early and the `sra` stays with its only use. Reach for it whenever a diff shows two shifts of one operand on opposite sides of an unrelated store; it is a named-intermediate lever of the `h-all-volatile-pins-straight-line` family, not a scheduler flag (measured the same round: `-fschedule-insns` on this cc1 produces a byte-identical .s, so the flag is a no-op for these units).

- id: s-volatile-store-forces-the-reload | keywords: volatile, store, reload, fold, immediate, global, cse, lh
  **A VOLATILE STORE OF THE IMMEDIATE KEEPS A LATER PLAIN READ OF THE SAME GLOBAL A REAL `lh`/`lw`; WITHOUT IT cc1 FOLDS THE RELOAD INTO THE STORED CONSTANT** (round 408, world_map/main/misc7 func_800828DC, parked 71.22% at 1668 of retail's 1672 bytes). Retail's camera-keyframe cases store `g_WorldMapViewRotationY = 0xD70` and then genuinely reload it (`lui v0,%hi / lh v0,%lo / nop / sll v0,12 / sw v0,0x54 / sw v0,0x3C`); with a plain store, gcc 2.7.2 forwards the store and emits `lui v0,0xd70000` instead - 34 instructions lost across five cases (383 insns / 1608 bytes). `*(volatile s16 *)&g_WorldMapViewRotationY = 0xD70;` emits the identical `sh` (with `.set volatile` around it) and the later read stays a load. Probed in isolation with cc1_probe.sh on the same unit: the plain store folds the load to `li $2,0x00d70000`, the volatile store gives `lh $3,g_WorldMapViewRotationY`. Reading through a pointer local (`s16 *p = &g; *p`) does NOT stop the fold. The same unit's func_800827EC park already relied on this for its zero stores. Check whether a "missing load" residue is really a store-forward before parking a body that looks one instruction short per case.

- id: h-volatile-load-costs-a-delay-slot-nop | keywords: volatile, store, reload, order, nop, delay-slot, cse, hoist
  **A VOLATILE STORE PAIR DOES KEEP A FOLLOWING VOLATILE RELOAD BELOW IT, BUT THAT LOAD THEN OWNS AN UNFILLABLE `nop` DELAY SLOT, so a body whose only residual is retail's store-then-reload order gains a byte per site and can no longer link** (round 411, battle/main/misc33 func_800BF3E8, XenoBattleTailCdk0, -fschedule-insns). Retail is `sw s0,0x74(s1) / sw s1,0x74(s0) / lw a0,%lo(g_BattleActorList) / lw v1,0xA8(s0)` with pState in `a1`; the body holding the reads in a `pLast` local and only the third read volatile hoists that reload above the two stores (82.89% at retail's exact 264 bytes / 66 insns), because a volatile load may cross non-volatile accesses. Making both stores volatile too (`*(void *volatile *)&p->pTargetSprite = pLast;`) reproduces the order exactly, and the reload comes out `lw` + `nop`: 67 insns / 268 bytes, 84.65 - one instruction over. Companion direction on the same body: with the stores volatile, a PLAIN reload is stored-forwarded away entirely (63 insns / 252 bytes, 85.27), so a volatile store does not restore a reload - only the volatile lvalue at the read does, and it then pays the nop. Both remaining spots there are one sched1 decision (retail hoists `move a2,s1` above the ra/s0 saves and gives the jal slot to the mask `sh`; our compile keeps `move a2,s1` for the slot), so park this class rather than buying the order with volatile.
- id: battle-per-store-volatile-word-index | keywords: volatile, reload, index, prim, pool, store, cse, address
  **A PRIM POOL ADDRESSED BY A WORD THAT RETAIL RE-READS BEFORE EVERY ONE OF 16 STORES IS A VOLATILE WORD READ, AND THE SPELLING THAT GETS IT UNDER AN ARRAY EXTERN IS A `volatile s32 *` LOCAL** (round 411, battle/main/misc17 func_80092B74, 65.04 -> 87.17%: 1020 -> 1252 bytes against retail's 1336). Retail loads the same 0x800CCB34 word 16 times as `lui v1,%hi(sym)` + `lw v1,%lo(sym)(v1)` - the direct-symbol macro form, so a SCALAR volatile declaration (misc11.c in this overlay declares `extern volatile s32 g_BattlePrimStartIndex;`). Under this file's `extern u8 g_BattlePrimStartIndex[]` (needed by five matched neighbours) the plain cast form `((s32 *)g_BattlePrimStartIndex)[0]` emits ONE shared `la` + `lw 0(reg)` that cse keeps across the stores - measured 65.04% - because gcc 2.6 does not treat a store through a pointer as an alias of the symbol. Adding a `volatile s32 *pPrimIndex = (volatile s32 *)g_BattlePrimStartIndex;` local restores the per-store reload (`lw 0(a1)` 16 times, 1 instruction short of retail's 2 each) and the rebuilt scale chain, worth 22 points; it also costs no frame slot here, since the pointer is live only after the last call site. Do NOT also keep a second counters local in the same function: the sixth callee-saved register moves the frame from 0x30 to 0x38 (86.55%).
- id: h-volatile-store-keeps-the-giv-address | keywords: volatile, store, base, giv, regalloc, delay-slot, reorg, pointer
  **A VOLATILE STORE'S ADDRESS IS NEVER STRENGTH-REDUCED, so it comes out off the struct base pointer where retail's plain store uses the loop's offset-N giv - routing the address through a NON-volatile pointer local used at EVERY site of the same field unconcitionally gives both: the giv base AND the position only the volatile buys** (round 417, slus_006.64/system/sound func_8003C4C4, XenoLibrary gcc 2.6.0, 99.96 -> 100.00 at retail's exact 548 bytes / 137 insns). Retail's tail is `sh a3,2(a0)` with `$a0 = &element->status_flags`, the loop's offset-2 giv (incremented by 0x158 in the latch beside the element base `$a1`), and the same word was loaded 17 instructions earlier through that giv; the plain `pElement->rampFlags = elementFlags;` does use `2(a0)` but reorg then sinks that store into the `bne` delay slot and pushes `addiu t2,t2,-1` past the 0x1000 arm (98.10, 8 differing lines), while every volatile lvalue keeps retail's position but forms the address off the base pointer (`sh a3,4(a1)`, 99.96 - `*(volatile u16*)&pElement->rampFlags`, `((volatile AudioElement*)pElement)->rampFlags` and the base+delta cast alike). The fix, one local and no other change: `u16* pFlags;` declared at the top, `pFlags = &pElement->status_flags;` as the loop body's FIRST statement, then `*pFlags` for the status_flags load, `*pFlags` for its store and `*(volatile u16*)(pFlags + 1)` for the rampFlags store - the local IS the giv, so the volatile store lands on the giv register, and being volatile it stays before the branch. Measured near-misses on the same body: a `volatile u16* pFlags` (then the status load itself reads `lhu t0,2(a1)`, 99.96), a non-volatile `pFlags[1] = elementFlags;` (98.10, the sinking store), and a pointer local used for the rampFlags store alone (the older 99.53 note - the local must cover the field's other sites to become the giv). Presets ruled out with try_flags: gcc-2.7.2-psx 95.76, 2.7.2-cdk 95.76, unit flags with -G0 99.96.

- id: h-store-escape-plus-value-locals | keywords: store, hoist, volatile, escape, address, locals, lw, lhu
  **A STORE-HOIST PARK CLOSES WHEN THE ESCAPED ADDRESS AND THE VALUE LOCALS ARE COMBINED: the escape alone fixes the store ORDER but narrows the load and swaps the sum's register, the locals alone leave the value hoisted** (round 417, battle/main/misc16 func_8008E430, 98.39 -> 100.00 at retail's exact 1600 bytes / 400 insns, XenoBattle260, after two earlier permuter runs - 1791 and 2129 iterations - found nothing). Retail stores x0 (`sh a1,0x1E8`) BEFORE y0 (`lw v1,0x30(sp) / addu a2,a2,v1 / addiu t0,a2,-8 / sh t0,0x1EA`); with x0 first in source cc1 2.6.0's cse2 hoists the y0 value's stack-argument load and its whole sum above the x0 store (98.20, 10 lines off), and with y0 first the two stores come out swapped (98.39, the park). `s32 *pArg4; pArg4 = &arg4;` plus one inline escaped read, `(bottom = (top = arg2 + *pArg4) - 8)`, keeps retail's x0-first order and costs NO frame slot or extra instruction (1600 bytes = retail's exact size, unlike the u16 `&arg4` home in `j-a-narrow-stack-argument-is`), but reads 99.01: the escape stops the hoist, the truncation then sits next to the load so combine narrows `lw` to `lhu`, and the sum lands in the load's register (`addu t0,t0,a2` where retail has `addu a2,a2,v1`). Giving the five values locals at their FIRST store - `x0 = (left = arg1 + 8)`, `y0 = (bottom = (top = arg2 + *pArg4) - 8)`, `x1 = (right = left + (half = (arg3 - 16) / 2))`, then plain `left`/`right`/`half`/`top + 8`/`bottom` uses - removes the narrow (the load is no longer adjacent to its truncation) and lands retail's exact register set (a1=left, a0=right, a3=half, a2=top, t0=bottom): 100.00. Try the two halves together on a hoist residual; they are not alternatives. Removing the escape from the final body re-measures 98.20, so the address-taking is load-bearing.

- id: h-do-while-is-the-store-barrier | keywords: do-while, store, order, hoist, barrier, volatile, group, li
  **A RUN OF CONSTANT STORES THAT RETAIL KEEPS AFTER A PRECEDING RUN OF STORES IS HELD THERE BY `do { } while (0)`, NOT BY `volatile`: gcc 2.7.2's pre-reload scheduler ignores a volatile store when deciding whether to hoist a plain store above it, so the volatile cast measures byte-identical while the do-while grouping reproduces retail's position** (round 417, world_map/main/misc1 func_80072238, parked: retail stores tpage=2, drawmode=4, the callback address, five zero flags, then `sw v1,g_AnimScriptExtraTicks` / `sw v1,g_WorldMapFadeActive` / `sw v0,callback`). Measured on that body, all 1892 bytes = retail's exact size: the two `= 1` stores written with `*(volatile s32 *)&` give output identical to the plain stores (99.81, pair hoisted to right after the DrawMode store); wrapping the same two in `do { } while (0)` keeps them after the five zero stores (99.46) and the residual shrinks to one instruction - the pair's `li`, which then lands after DrawMode in `v0` where retail hoists it to the block head in `v1`; a `void*` local for the callback address and an `s32` local for the 1 both reproduce their positions (lui/addiu early) at the same 99.46, and the local's establishment position in source changes nothing because sched places the `li`; a 3-minute permuter run from the do-while body found nothing (1242 iterations, base=250). So reach for the do-while on a store-hoist residual BEFORE volatile, and expect the remaining tie to be which of two hoisted address/constant chains sched emits first.

- id: h-a-pointer-cast-of-a-scalar | keywords: cse, load, merge, volatile, pointer, cast, struct, probe
  **A NON-VOLATILE POINTER OR ARRAY CAST OF A SCALAR GLOBAL (`*(u32 *)&g_Sym`, `((u32 *)&g_Sym)[0]`) IS A DIFFERENT cse KEY FROM THE PLAIN `g_Sym` LOAD, because the component access sets MEM_IN_STRUCT_P, so a body that reads it twice keeps TWO loads in the direct `lui dst,%hi / lw dst,%lo(dst)` form where every plain spelling is cse'd to one** (round 420, battling/main/misc12 func_80088E90, parked 96.84% with the read spelled `*(volatile s32 *)&`). Measured with `cc1_probe.sh` on this unit's flags, four tiny functions reading one global twice (once feeding a `divu`, once to a second store): plain `b = g_Mask` emits ONE `lw` (the two reads merge), `b = *(u32 *)&g_Mask` and `b = ((u32 *)&g_Mask)[0]` emit TWO. Watch the boundary: inside func_80088E90's `for (;;)` body the same cast spelling measured 800 bytes / 200 insns - cc1 merged the pair there anyway (retail is 816 / 204) - so the probe result does not transfer into a big loop, and the only spelling that keeps both loads in the unit is the volatile one, which pays for it with an address-in-a-register form (`lui a1 / addiu a1,a1,0 / lw s2,0(a1)`) instead of retail's direct form. Use the probe when you need to know whether two same-address loads CAN be kept apart; do not expect it to hold in the unit without re-measuring.
- id: h-pointer-cast-does-not-split-a-pointer-global | keywords: cse, load, merge, cast, pointer, global, reload, volatile
  **THE NON-VOLATILE POINTER/ARRAY CAST THAT SPLITS A SCALAR GLOBAL'S TWO READS DOES NOT SPLIT A SECOND READ OF A POINTER-VALUED GLOBAL: for `extern T *g_P;` every non-volatile spelling still cse's into one register and only a volatile lvalue emits the load** (round 424, battle/main/misc33 func_800BF3E8, XenoBattleTailCdk0; retail names `g_BattleActorList` twice around two 0x74 struct stores, `lw s0` then `lw a0`, keeping s0 for the flag loads). Measured on that body, all 63 insns / 252 bytes / 85.27 vs retail's 66 / 264: `*(BattleActorSprite **)&g_BattleActorList`, `((BattleActorSprite **)&g_BattleActorList)[0]`, a local holding `&g_BattleActorList` dereferenced, and `*ppList` for the first read with the named read last. So h-a-pointer-cast-of-a-scalar's split is specific to a scalar (`u32`/`s16`) target: the cse key gains nothing when the load's type is already a pointer. The volatile cast does emit the load (85.91 with all registers right once the reload sits between the two stores in source), so the residual on this shape is scheduler order, not the folding.

- id: h-a-store-after-a-jal-keeps-the-saved-base | keywords: store, jal, sched-insns, base, argument, copy, hoist, a0
  **A STORE PLACED AFTER THE CALL IN SOURCE IS NOT HOISTED ABOVE THE `jal` BY THE PRE-RELOAD SCHEDULER, AND IT KEEPS EVERY EARLIER STORE OF THE SAME POINTER ON THE CALLEE-SAVED BASE - so if moving a store after the call does flip retail's `sw/sb (a0)` to `(s0)` and the score DROPS, the residual is the store BASE (the call's argument copy merged into the pointer), never statement order** (round 422, battle/main/misc32 func_800BDC14, XenoBattleTailCdk0, re-measured 91.60 -> 81.60 at retail's exact 100 bytes / 25 insns). Retail is `bgez / sw v0,0x88(s0)` then `li v1,16 / lui a1 / move a0,s0 / lbu v0,0x83(s0) / sw v1,0x88(s0) / ori / andi / jal / sb v0,0x83(s0)`; writing the block as flags-store, call, timer-store does give `lbu/sb (s0)` and lands the `sb` in the jal delay slot, but the timer store then stays AFTER the call (sched1 will not move an independent store across a call), so the order cannot be bought that way and the shape is strictly worse. Preset screen on the same body: gcc-2.6.0-psx 76.76, gcc-2.7.2-psx under the unit flags compile-failed, so this class is not a preset question. Companion negative result: `pTask->timer = nextTimer;` before the call scores 91.60 with the ORDER already retail's and only the two/three store bases at `$a0` instead of `$s0`.

- id: h-volatile-s16-read-emits-lhu-not-lh | keywords: volatile, lhu, lh, reload, fold, s16, extern, store
  **ON cc1 2.7.2 A `volatile` READ OF AN `s16` EXTERN COMES OUT `lhu`, NOT `lh`** (round 424, world_map/main/misc7 func_80081470, XenoWorldMapDiv): retail re-reads a halfword the preceding statement stored, so the body needs a barrier (`*pViewY = -0x200;` through a `volatile s16 *`, then `viewY = *pViewY << 12;`), and the reload is emitted `lhu $3,g_WorldMapViewRotationY` even though the extern is `s16` - the shifted value is bit-identical, so it is a one-instruction residual, not a wrong type to chase. A plain read after the volatile store folds (that body was 4 bytes / 1 instruction short) and a plain read of the neighbouring symbol reorders the store below the load, which is silently wrong code.

- id: h-cast-store-reuses-the-volatile-index-register | keywords: store, order, volatile, cast, index, sched2, delay-slot, register
  **WRITING A STORE AS A CAST TO A COMPUTED ADDRESS (`*(s32 *)((u8 *)G + idx * 0x48 + 0x20) = v;`) INSTEAD OF THE TYPED `G[idx].field = v;` MOVES THE STORED VALUE'S TEMPORARY INTO THE SAME REGISTER AS THE VOLATILE INDEX READ, which reproduces retail's phase for a whole block of stores and pays for it with one base-derivation instruction per store** (round 428, field/main/misc8 func_80081C54, Default preset, parked 69.69%). Retail reads a volatile index per statement and keeps the value load after the index's last use because BOTH are `$v1` (that is why retail has an unfilled `nop` after every `lui/lw` of the index); the typed spelling gives the index `$v1` and the value `$a0`, so sched2 fills the index load's delay slot with the value load and all 15 singleton stores come out one instruction early - 63.95% at retail's EXACT 194 insns / 776 bytes. The cast spelling puts both in `$v1` and the phase becomes retail's, but gcc then materialises the ring base in a register once and derives `addu $5,$4,<offset>` per statement: 69.56%, 780 bytes. Retail mixes the two forms (symbol+`$at` addressing for the nine singleton offsets, a `la a1,<sym>`/`addiu`-derived base for the three consecutive-offset stores and the loop), so on a diff whose only defect is "value load before the volatile index read", try the cast store for the whole block first and then decide per group which addressing form you need.

- id: k-an-address-local-pinned-at-the-top | keywords: address, pointer, local, symbol_ref, remat, callee-saved, case-block, frame
  **A POINTER LOCAL TO AN ABSOLUTE GLOBAL IS FOLDED BACK TO THE BARE SYMBOL WHEN IT IS ASSIGNED WHERE IT IS USED, BUT PINNED FOR THE WHOLE FUNCTION WHEN IT IS INITIALISED ONCE AT THE TOP - and retail's shape is usually neither: it forms the address at the head of each case block and keeps it across that block's calls, which no spelling reached here** (round 429, world_map/main/misc5 func_8007C7D8, XenoWorldMapDiv, parked 74.45% at 1176 bytes / 294 insns against retail's 1172 / 293). Retail holds `&g_WorldMapScrollTargetZ` in `$s0` and `&g_WorldMapCameraTargetZ` in `$s1` across the `WorldMapWrapDelta` call and re-reads/stores through them afterwards (4 save/restore slots, frame 48). Measured: `pScrollTargetZ = &g_X;` written at the head of the case that uses it compiles BYTE-IDENTICALLY to the plain `g_X` body (gcc folds the pointer and rematerialises `lui` + `addiu` around the call, 1176 / 294); the same two pointers initialised at the top of the function pin them but hoist both formations into the prologue and buy an extra `$s4` - EXACT retail size 1172 / 293 at a lower objdiff (73.16 against 74.45), i.e. the size is right and the form sites are wrong. Note the contrast with `func_8007ADD4` in the same unit, where `-G0` DOES rescue the pointer shape (86.32): here `-G0` is 74.45, `2.7.2-cdk -G0` 73.67 and `2.6.0` 74.00, so screen the preset on the stubs that show this pattern before believing either direction.

- id: h-volatile-alias-forces-one-guard-store-reload | keywords: volatile, cse, reload, guard, alias, extern, asm-label, load
  **A GUARD'S LOAD THAT cc1 2.6.0 CSE'S INTO THE FIRST STORE'S LOAD CAN BE BROKEN AT ONE SITE ONLY WITH A SECOND VOLATILE DECLARATION OF THE SAME SYMBOL UNDER AN ASM LABEL** (round 431, battle/main/misc23 func_800A5EB4, 99.16 -> 100.00 at retail's exact 1424 bytes / 356 insns, XenoBattle260Div0). Retail loads `g_BattlePartPrimBuffers` for the null test and loads it AGAIN for the block's first byte store (`lui/lw/nop`, `beqz`, `addiu` in the delay slot, then `lui/lw/nop` + base-first `addu v0,v0,a2`), while loop 2's first store reuses the guard's register on purpose (its `addu` sits in the `beqz` delay slot) - so a `volatile` extern is wrong (it reloads both, 359 insns) and `*(u8 * volatile *)&g` is wrong too (volatile forces an INDIRECT address: `lui/addiu/lw`, 357 / 1428). What reproduces retail is keeping the plain `extern u8 *g;` for the guards and every other site and adding `extern u8 * volatile gVolatile __asm__("g");` used at that ONE store (`pSlot = gVolatile; pSlot += i * 0x18; pDst[0] = pSlot[0x284];`): an asm-labelled scalar still addresses the symbol directly (`lui/lw`, not `lui/addiu/lw`), volatility keeps it out of the cse table, and the two-statement in-place form keeps the base-first `addu`. The reloc name is unchanged, so objdiff and the link are unaffected. Reach for it when a diff's only residual is one missing `lui/lw/nop` before a store whose guard already loaded the same symbol.

- id: h-a-return-constant-leads-the-store-group-only-in-retail | keywords: store, order, return, constant, li, do-while, volatile, probe
  **A RETAIL TAIL THAT OPENS WITH `addiu v0,zero,<ret>` AND THEN STORES EVERY FIELD WITH THE CONSTANTS ROTATED INTO `$v1`/`$a0` CANNOT BE SPELLED FROM A PLAIN `return <const>`: cc1 emits the return set AFTER the last store that needs a register, and cse deletes any constant local that would move it - a cc1 probe matrix settles it in five compiles** (round 434, world_map/main/misc6 func_8007FC8C, parked 93.41% at retail's exact 164 bytes / 41 insns, XenoWorldMapDiv; the rest of the function is byte-identical). `tools/scripts/cc1_probe.sh` on the tail only (a 7-store group and the return), each run ~10 s: plain source emits `li $2,x/li $3,z/sw...` with the `3` AFTER the last register-needing store and the pStateFrames store glued to its own `li` at 4th; `return 3` INSIDE a `do {} while (0)` gives the `3` early but the same 4th-place glue; a `volatile` pStateFrames store does NOT pin it (a volatile store may move EARLIER past non-volatile ones - it only ever blocks sinking, never hoisting); and the do-block AROUND the stores (return outside) gives retail's exact store order with the `3` last. So retail's leading `li` means its RTL really had the return set before the group (a source-level value that survived cse, which no `return const` is), and the two reachable bodies are 93.41 (order right, `li` late) and 88.66 (either `li` early or order wrong). Park the 93.41 body; the preset is not the lever (try_flags --all under 2.7.2-cdk gives this body the same 93.41 while every matched function in the unit drops to 96-99.67).

- id: h-per-vertex-order-hoists-the-constants | keywords: store, order, li, constant, hoist, register, sched1, quad
  **WHEN A RUN OF STRUCT-FIELD STORES HAS EVERY INSTRUCTION RIGHT BUT THE CONSTANT `li`s SIT AT THE WRONG STORES AND IN DIFFERENT REGISTERS (`li t0,208` hoisted in retail, ours reloading `li v0` at each store), THE CAUSE IS THE STATEMENT GROUP ORDER: retail's column-major EMITTED order (all four y, then all four x) is what the scheduler produced, not what was typed - write the assignments per vertex (x0,y0,x1,y1,...) and sched1 takes retail's order, with the registers following and no other edit** (round 435, world_map/main/misc1 func_80073E30, 97.23 -> MATCH at retail's exact 648 bytes / 162 instructions, XenoWorldMap). The parked body listed the POLY_FT4 fields in retail's emitted store order (setPolyFT4, y0,y1,y2,y3, x0,x2,x1,x3, u0,v0,u1,v1,...) and scored 97.23 with 16 differing lines, every one of them a `li` placement plus the v0/t0/v1 choice; the same fields through the typed struct (`pPrim->x0 = 208;`) produced a byte-identical instruction stream at 95.81 (the drop is only base+offset relocations against `D_8009C5C0` instead of splat's per-field symbols, which link identically), and only the per-vertex source order closed it. So a straight-line block of `li`+`store` rows whose stores are already in retail's order but whose constants are misplaced wants the natural per-object statement order, not the emitted order - and it is worth more than a permuter round on this shape.

- id: h-a-constant-store-is-hoisted-one-statement | keywords: store, order, constant, zero, hoist, shift, sched, load-delay
  **cc1 FLOATS A CONSTANT STORE (`sh $0,<off>`) UP TO JUST AFTER THE NEAREST PRECEDING MEMORY LOAD AND ABOVE THE VALUE STORE THAT FOLLOWS IT, SO A BLOCK OF `sh`-OF-ZERO STORES HAS TO BE WRITTEN ONE VALUE STORE LATER THAN RETAIL'S EMITTED POSITION - writing the emitted order reproduces nothing** (round 447, world_map/main/misc4 func_80079778, parked 99.74% at retail's exact 2292 bytes / 573 insns, XenoWorldMap). Retail's post-switch store group emits A2=unk50, AA=unk58, BA=0, B8=0, B2=unk60, BC=unk60; the four bodies measured on the same source, each one edit apart: statement order A2, AA, BA, B2, B8, BC gives A2, BA, AA, B8, B2, BC (99.31); A2, AA, BA, B8, B2, BC (retail's EMITTED order) gives A2, BA, B8, AA, B2, BC (99.04); `volatile` on the two zero stores hoists them further, to before the AA store (99.04); and A2, AA, B2, BA, B8, BC - the zeros one value store later than retail's emission - gives retail's exact A2, AA, BA, B8, B2, BC (99.74, +0.43). So model the zero stores as "get out as soon as the preceding load's value is available" and place them AFTER the following value store in the source; `volatile` pins a store against SINKING only, never against this hoist. Residual after the fix on the same function: the three value setups of the following call (a casted scratch address, a `move` and an `ori`) fill the block's three load-delay slots in the order ori/move/addiu where retail's is addiu/move/ori - same instructions, rotated, permuter base=280 best=280 over 1151 iterations, presets cdk 90.32 and 2.6.0 98.16.

- id: h-empty-do-while-between-two-store-chains-stops-the-extra-reload-hoist | keywords: store, hoist, reload, order, do-while, barrier, sched2, allocator, register, tie
  **TWO STORE CHAINS THAT EACH RE-READ THE SAME ALIASED GLOBAL COME OUT WITH BOTH READS HOISTED ABOVE THE FIRST STORE'S PREVIOUS STATEMENT - one extra live register, a mirrored caller-saved pair and 4 bytes short of retail; an EMPTY `do { } while (0);` between the two chains restores retail's one-reload-per-chain order AND the whole register picture, taking the body from 86.75 to 99.31 at retail's exact size, and the last instruction then falls to a permuter-found variable** (round 453, battle/main/misc13 func_80088490, 80 insns / 320 bytes = retail's, Default preset). Retail stores and re-reads `g_BattleSlotRecords[ofs]` once per statement (store slot, read, store the 0 byte, chain 1 store, read, chain 2 store, read, ...) because every store may alias the next read; our body hoisted the SECOND read above the intervening store, kept two reloads live (`lbu $3` + `lbu $2`), which pushed `ofs` from `$v1` to `$a0`, the constant 1 from `$a0` to `$a1`, the param-table base from `$a0` to `$a1` and produced `addu v0,v0,a1` where retail has base-first `addu v0,a0,v0` - one barrier fixed all of them at once. Also measured on the same body: making the intervening store `*(volatile u8 *)(D_800C3EB5 + ofs) = 0;` changes NOTHING (86.75, byte-identical), a local `u8 rec` re-read at each site makes gcc CSE the four reloads away (77 insns / 308 bytes, 84.94), `do {} while (0)` between the two PARAM-TABLE chains costs 7 points (92.00, 18 lines), and swapping the two usage stores in the source does change WHICH of them gcc emits first (that is how retail's `D_800D301D`-before-`g_BattleCharSlotUsage` order was recovered, 99.06 -> 99.31 together with the barrier). The final instruction was the permuter's: a `s32 stride` local assigned `8` before the two param-table chains and used as `g_BattleSlotRecords[ofs] * stride` (PERMUTER_ZERO in 344 iterations, and the same find spelled with a dedicated, meaningfully named local instead of its dead `sel = 8;` also prints MATCH 100.00) - the multiplier becoming a pseudo is what lets the base land in `$a0` and reuse it in place, which no constant spelling reaches.

## switch, jump tables and exit shape

symptoms: case body order, jump table vs chain, early-exit fold
try: switch jtbl case exit
- id: i-a-shared-case-exit-block-carries-the-globals | keywords: switch, case, exit, shared, fallback, block, store, semantic
  **WHEN TWO `return` STATEMENTS OF ONE CASE ARE ONE BLOCK IN RETAIL, THAT BLOCK USUALLY DOES MORE THAN THE DUPLICATED CALL - read its stores before writing the two exits; respelling the case so gcc merges its exits into one trailing block was worth 6.3 points on its own** (round 446, world_map/main/misc14 func_80095414, 64.84 -> 71.12% at 2152 bytes / 538 vs retail 2240 / 576, XenoWorldMapDiv). Retail's SEARCH case has two "give up and go to the terrain" exits (`beqz s4` after `WorldMapFindSpriteSet`, `beq s2,s4` after the check loop) and both land on ONE block, .L8009570C, which calls `WorldMapResolveStepAgainstTerrain` and then stores -1 into BOTH `D_8009C16C` and `D_8009C840` before returning. The parked body had `return WorldMapResolveStepAgainstTerrain(...);` at each exit - two instructions missing and no shared block - so the whole search half read as mismatched. Spelling it as `found = 0; if (count != 0) { check loop } if (count != found) { second loop; returns } ret = Resolve(...); D_8009C16C = -1; D_8009C840 = -1; return ret;` gives exactly one copy of the tail (the nesting, not a `goto`, is what makes cc1 merge it) and that half then matched instruction for instruction. So: count the exits a case has in the .s, and when retail has fewer blocks than the body has `return`s, diff the body of the shared block against the call the body duplicates.
- id: i-a-continue-arm-belongs-outside-the-switch | keywords: switch, case, continue, chain, order, root, default, if
  **A `switch` ARM WHOSE BODY IS `continue` MUST BE AN `if (...) continue;` BEFORE THE SWITCH: as a `case` it becomes part of cc1's decision tree and moves the whole compare chain (and costs 2 instructions), as an `if` the switch is left with the dense case set and the chain comes out as retail's** (round 445, battle/main/misc13 func_80086F98, 91.49 -> 93.69% at 2128 bytes / 532 insns against retail 2120 / 530). Retail's sprite loop tests `0xFF` first with `ori v0,1` in the first compare's delay slot, then `1`, then `slti v0,2`, then `0`/`2` - that is the tree for cases {0,1,2} only. With `case BATTLE_COMMAND_NONE: continue;` in the switch (four cases incl. 0xFF) cc1 roots the tree at 1 and puts the 0xFF test after the `slti`, adding an extra `j`+slot; moving the arm out as `if (g_BattleCommandQueue[0x2CC + i] == BATTLE_COMMAND_NONE) { continue; }` reproduces retail's chain instruction for instruction. Also measured on that body and dead: `default: continue;` with cases 0/1/2 (89.83, 2092 bytes), `default:` on the last case arm (89.46, 2092), and the case labels written in retail's chain order (91.48 - cc1 sorts the list, source order is not the lever). Symptom to pass to lever.py: a diff whose switch chain tests the sentinel value in the wrong position, or whose tree root is not the case value retail tests first.
- id: i-two-case-tree-root-is-the-lowest-value | keywords: switch, case, tree, root, slti, order, two-case, 260
  **FOR A TWO-CASE `switch` cc1 MAKES THE LOWEST CASE VALUE THE TREE ROOT NO MATTER WHAT ORDER THE `case` LABELS ARE WRITTEN IN, so when retail's root is the HIGHEST value followed by an `slti` range test on the subtree, that shape is not reachable from a two-case switch - and the constant it compares against is the thing to look at next, because retail keeps it in a callee-saved register** (round 430, menu/main/misc8_r2 func_801D9F98, parked 95.67% at 1288 vs retail 1296 bytes / 322 vs 324 insns, XenoMenu260 = gcc 2.6.0). Retail's tree for cases {0,2} on `g_MenuOverlayIndex` is `beq x,s5(2) -> case2 / slti v0,x,3 / beqz v0 -> end / bnez x -> end / [case 0]`, and `s5` holds `ori s5,zero,0x2` from the loop preheader reused at three sites in the loop - that register is exactly retail's 8th callee-saved save/restore pair and its 0x48 frame, and it is worth 8 bytes against the 0x40 frame the body gets by rematerialising `li v0,0x2` per site. Both source orders measured and NO help: `case 0` first and `case 2` first both give `beqz v0,x -> case0 / li v0,0x2 / beq x,v0 -> case2 / j end` (cc1 sorts the case list, so the root is not source-order controlled), as does `default: break;`, and an explicit empty `case 1: break;` makes it worse (328 insns, 95.02). The if/else spelling of the same two arms tests 2 first like retail but drops the range test entirely (319 insns, 95.21), so the switch is the closer base. Useful control in the same body: the four-case switch on `g_Menu->input` (1,3,4,5) DOES get retail's `slti v0,a0,4` tree under 2.6.0, so the tree-with-range-test method is reachable - just not for two cases. Other measured spellings here: `(MenuBeginCardOperation() & 0xFF) != 0` is retail's `andi` (the bare call is one insn short), a ternary argument folds branchless into `xori/sltiu/negu/andi` (+4 insns) where `choice = 7; if (x != 2) choice = 0;` gives retail's `beq` + `li` in the delay slot, and moving `s32 result;` to the top of the local list changes nothing (95.67 both ways). gcc-2.7.2 scores 94.37 on this body, so the unit preset is not the answer.

- id: i-code-after-the-switch-may | keywords: switch, case, trailing, epilogue, target, beq, default, layout
  **CODE THAT LOOKS LIKE IT FOLLOWS A `switch` MAY BELONG INSIDE THE LAST ARM: compare the target of the EMPTY case and of the dispatch default against the trailing block and the epilogue, because if they land on the EPILOGUE the source's last case ran to the end of the function** (round 412, battle/main/misc11_b func_80081B58, park 98.51 -> 99.32 with the byte count going from 1352 to retail's exact 1356 / 339 insns). Retail's dispatch is `beq v1,5,<epilogue>` and `j <epilogue>`, and the two `if (gaugeValue == 0)` / `if (unk2E5 != 0)` blocks sit between the last case's arm tail and the epilogue, i.e. they are the last case's own trailing statements: with them written after the switch (the obvious spelling) every non-taken path executes them, all three tree branches target the trailing block instead of the epilogue, and the function is 4 bytes SHORT. Moving the two `if`s inside `case 6:` before its `break` fixed every one of those targets at once. The tell is cheap: dump the retail `.s` for the switch and read the TWO targets of the empty case / default - equal to the block that follows the switch means "after the switch", equal to the epilogue's `lw ra` means "inside the last arm". Same round's second finding on the same body: an unused `s32` return type is what keeps retail's `nop` in the empty case's `beq` delay slot (levers.md c-stolen-delay-slot-retail), worth 99.03 -> 99.32 there.
- id: i-state-arm-stores-its | keywords: switch, case, store, compare, cse, scroll, arm
  **WHEN A CASE ARM COMPARES AGAINST A COMPUTED VALUE AND THEN STORES THE SAME KIND OF VALUE, SPELL THE STORE WITH THE COMPARE'S OWN EXPRESSION AND PUT IT BEFORE THE INCREMENT** - retail then computes that value ONCE and stores it from the compare's register, and all four arms share one store tail. battle/main/misc15 func_8008B478 (round 396, gcc 2.6.0, 1176 -> 1168 = retail's exact bytes, 292 insns, objdiff 99.97 with only the jtbl operand left): the parked body stored `g_BattleCursorScrollY = j * 16` AFTER the arm's `j++`, which is the same value as the compare's `(j + 1) * 16` but not the same tree, so gcc rematerialised `andi/sll` in each arm (+2 instructions, and the park spent two rounds blaming the allocator). Writing `g_BattleCursorScrollY = (j + 1) * 16;` as the arm's FIRST statement, before `j++`, makes cse share one register, and doing it in EVERY arm is what lets gcc merge the four identical `sw/sb/andi` tails into retail's shared `.L` block. The mirror trap is real: the same fix applied to only one arm measures worse (1192), because the arms then no longer end identically. A `s32 nextY` local holding the target is NOT the same spelling (1192) - the shared expression has to be the compare's, not a variable.
- id: i-list-the-even-case-values | keywords: switch, jtbl, density, chain, default, case, sparse
  **A SWITCH OVER A BIT-COMBINATION NEEDS ITS UNUSED VALUES LISTED EXPLICITLY OR cc1 2.7.2 EMITS A COMPARISON CHAIN INSTEAD OF THE JUMP TABLE RETAIL HAS** (round 399, world_map/main/misc11 func_8008C364). The value is three boolean bits (`partyMembers[index] != 0xFF`, a per-actor angle `>= 0x400`, `gearRide[index] == 1`) and only 1/3/5/7 have bodies; written as `case 1: ... case 3: case 7: ... case 5: ... default:` cc1 produced a `beq/slti` chain at 60.90%. Naming the dead values (`case 0: case 2: case 4: case 6:` sharing the default body) makes the label set cover 0..7 and cc1 emits retail's 8-word table at 91.52% - the table's even entries are exactly the default block. The switch's range check and the table itself then match retail word for word. Also measured on that function: retail's nine-shape block layout was reproducible as long as the two `*(volatile s32 *)&pActor->x` / `*(volatile s16 *)&pActor->unk24` pins from lever a-inline-field-reads-beat-locals' neighbours were kept (93.26 -> 95.87%, 460 bytes = retail's exact size); the residual is one branch POLARITY - retail reaches a shared `result = 3` block with `beq` from case 1 and keeps its own copy at the value-0 position, cc1 emits `bne` and merges the block into case 1's region - which no case order (even-first, case-1-first, duplicated tails) moved.
- id: i-case-0-label-keeps-table-base | keywords: switch, jtbl, sltiu, case, default, index, table, bound
  **A `case 0:` LABEL SHARING THE DEFAULT BODY IS WHAT KEEPS RETAIL'S UNBIASED JUMP TABLE; without it cc1 folds the index and emits one entry fewer** (round 400, world_map/main/misc6 func_80080370, 97.15 -> 99.92 at retail's exact 520 bytes, the only residual then the jtbl operand). The switch covers 1..0x40 with no case-0 body: retail reads `lh v1,0x20(s0)` / `sltiu v0,v1,0x41` and indexes a 65-word table whose slot 0 is the default block, while `default: break;` alone makes cc1 subtract the low bound - `addiu v0,v0,-1 / sll / sra / sltiu v0,v1,64`, a 64-word table and an `lhu` selector. Writing `case 0:` directly above `default: break;` restores retail's `lh` + `sltiu 0x41` + 65 entries with every other instruction already identical. Count the retail table's words against the case count first, then add the `case 0:`.
- id: i-gcc-emits-switch-case | keywords: switch, exit, case
  **gcc 2.6.0 emits switch case BODIES IN SOURCE ORDER and gives the fall-through arm to the FIRST-LISTED case.** So when retail inlines one arm (bne -> default plus the body) and jumps to the others, the listing order of the case labels is the lever, not the shape of the switch: battle/misc18 closed from 97.11% by listing 0x8000, 0x4000, 0x1000 (not ascending) and putting the 0xC group first.
- id: i-leading-continue-inlines-the-fail-block | keywords: block, order, continue, nested, fail, exit, if-else
  **A `if (a == 0) {fail} else if (b == 0) {fail} else {main; continue;} rows[i] = 0;` body makes gcc 2.7.2 lay BOTH fail bodies INLINE at the top with inverted `bnez` branches (two extra `j` + slot fills); writing the same chain NESTED with the POSITIVE outer test and the main body inside it - `if (a != 0) { if (b != 0) { main; rows = 1; } else { failB; rows = 0; } } else { failA; rows = 0; }` - puts them OUT OF LINE at the end, in retail's order, with the two identical `rows = 0` blocks cross-jumped into one** (round 394, menu/main/misc8_r2 func_801DA5BC: 91.33% -> 93.54% for the nested form with the inner test `== 0`, -> 95.44% with the inner `!= 0` so the main body is generated before its else, 1024 vs retail's 1012 bytes). The block layout follows the RTL generation order of the if/else arms, so in general: write the arm retail falls through FROM first (the `!=`/positive test with the body) and the fail arm as the `else`. The same unit's 8 already-matched functions stayed 100.00 across all three bodies.

- id: i-or-condition-sense-decides-the-arm | keywords: block, order, if-else, arm, sense, or, and, negate, exit
  **THE SENSE OF AN `||`/`&&` GUARD DECIDES WHICH ARM'S TESTS ARE INVERTED, AND RETAIL'S TESTS USUALLY POINT AT THE ARM IT LAID OUT FIRST** (round 402, slus_006.64/psyq/libgpu_b func_8004574C, 74.66 -> 100.00 at retail's exact 656 bytes / 164 insns, first try after the flip). Both arms of the `isbg` block are ~10 statements of duplicated GP0 emission, so a wrong arm order costs a full-body diff; retail is `bnez v0,L_armA` (x test), `beqz v0,L_armB` (w test), arm A inline, `j` end, arm B last. Writing the guard in the NEGATED sense (`if ((x & 0x3F) == 0 && (w & 0x3F) == 0) { armB } else { armA }`, i.e. the arm physical order retail has) makes cc1 invert BOTH tests and jump past the inline arm on the negative result (`bnez` x -> armA far away, `bnez` w -> armA), which reads as a 75% body with the arms swapped and every store in the wrong half. Writing the POSITIVE sense for the arm that comes first (`if ((x & 0x3F) != 0 || (w & 0x3F) != 0) { armA } else { armB }`) gives retail's `bnez`-to-A / `beqz`-to-B exactly. So: read which arm the retail tests BRANCH to versus fall into, and put that arm's condition in the positive form in the `if`.
- id: i-early-exit-gcc-folds | keywords: switch, exit, cast, delay, goto
  **An early exit that gcc folds to a constant is fixed by one shared exit.** Replace the early returns with a goto to a done label and a single return of the result variable at the end: gcc then copies that variable into $v0 in every branch delay slot as retail does, instead of proving the value is zero (battle/misc4 went 98.55 -> 99.78, and it also stopped gcc re-deriving a cast operand).
- id: i-if-c-return-x | keywords: switch, exit, delay, epilogue, permuter
  **`if (c) { return X; }` is a LAYOUT trap**: 2.7.2's `-fthread-jumps` threads the branch to the epilogue and folds the return-value copy into the branch's delay slot, which is shorter than retail's inline block. Wrapping the rest of the body in an `else` stops the threading, and an empty `do { } while (0);` as the LAST statement of the if block (after the return) is what produces retail's exact layout. Both are permuter finds; neither looks like source, and both are load-bearing.
- id: i-shared-block-reached-through | keywords: switch, exit, goto
  **A shared block reached through a label beats if/else for block ORDER** (round 320, `field/misc11` func_80092894 48.90 -> 91.82): `if (radius < distance) goto walk; arrived: <arrival>; return 0; walk: <test>; if (stuck < MAX) { ...; return -1; } goto arrived;` puts the arrival block physically between the test and the walking arm and makes the give-up test branch BACKWARDS into it, which is what retail does and no if/else arrangement reproduces.
- id: i-case-label-what-unbalances | keywords: switch, exit, case, cc1, li, slti
  **A `case 0:` label can be what unbalances a switch tree** (round 323): retail's chain (`beq 1; slti <2; bnez end; li 2; ...`) is what cc1 2.7.2 emits for FOUR case nodes, and adding `case 0: break;` (a value that does nothing, same as default) converts a balanced pivot-2 tree into retail's chain. It is the node COUNT, not the label order - so when a switch's control flow matches but its tree shape does not, count nodes before trying orderings.
- id: i-check-jtbl-owner-before | keywords: switch, exit, volatile
  **Check for a `jtbl_` owner BEFORE carving an island out of a unit.** round 330a tried the round-314 menu/misc7 procedure on `battle/misc19` (whose park note said one function is 100.00 under 2.6.0 only): the split produced correct runs and the known chunking bugs were fixed by hand, but the LINK then failed with ``undefined reference to jtbl_800704A0`` from a stub in the new tail run, because carving the `c` range moves the stub's asm into a new directory while battle's jump tables live in a shared rodata blob. Splitting is safe only in a unit that owns its rodata (or has had `own_rodata.py` applied) and whose stubs own no `jtbl_`. Also: a `volatile` READ is cheap enough that it should be tried before ANY split, since it beats the split entirely when the delta is a folded load.
- id: i-constant-original-loads-once | keywords: switch, exit, li
  A constant the original loads ONCE into a call-clobbered register before a switch ('li v1,0xA0', 'li a2,0x70): assign it to a source local before the switch; a literal gives a per-block li.
- id: i-case-body-order-switch | keywords: switch, exit, case
  CASE-BODY ORDER IN A 'switch' IS SOURCE ORDER under this preset: list the case labels in retail's body order and the arms come out in that order, reproducing its cross-jumped shared tails (round 221, battle/misc26 func_800B1720: 78.81 -> 99.71 by listing the labels in retail's order; world_map/misc12 saw the same). Same function: ONE STATEMENT PER ARITHMETIC STEP ('kind = code & MASK; kind |= ...;', 'abr = (mode - 1) & 3; abr <<= 5;') stops gcc coalescing the result back into the source register.
- id: i-switch-block-order-follows | keywords: switch, case, order, jtbl, block, duplicated, tail
  THE BLOCK ORDER IS THE SOURCE ORDER OF THE CASE LABELS WHILE THE JUMP TABLE'S ENTRY ORDER IS BY CASE VALUE, so a retail table whose arms sit in an odd physical order is reproduced by listing the labels in that odd order (round 398, menu/main/misc3 func_801CA750, 2.6.0/XenoMenu260, 368 bytes = retail's exact size, objdiff 99.89 with only `jtbl_` vs `.rodata` left). Retail's six arms sit `.L794`(4), `.L79C`(5), `.L7A4`(0), `.L7CC`(2), `.L7F4`(1), `.L81C`(3, fall-through); listing 0,1,2,3,4,5 measures 90.00% at 372 bytes with the two `result = n; break;` arms appended after case 3 and a duplicated tail, and listing exactly 4,5,0,2,1,3 reproduces retail instruction for instruction. Read the arms' physical order off the `.s` before writing the switch; do not assume ascending, and do not read the table's word order as the source order.
- id: i-two-more-exit-shape | keywords: switch, exit, case, frame, goto
  Two more exit-shape levers measured in rounds 214-215: an early return that retail keeps OUT OF LINE is reproduced by a `goto` to one shared exit with a `ret` variable (func_80045C10 went 0.00 -> 100.00 with it after the block order had resisted every if/else spelling), and the mirror case - retail cross-jumping two identical `return n` at the same block level - is fixed by NESTING the second one inside its branch (func_80046DB4: `if (n == 0) return 1; return n;` inside the not-ready arm, plus a final `return n;`; the duplicate is load-bearing). A `switch` on a NARROW index (`u_char v = mode & 7;` then `switch (v)`) is what makes gcc 2.7.2 emit retail's balanced case tree instead of a comparison chain (func_80046C58 73.5 -> 98.6%), and an unused local is the lever when retail allocates a frame it never stores into (func_80045A34).
- id: i-switch-becomes-jump-table | keywords: switch, exit
  A `switch` becomes a jump table in .rodata; `if/else` chains do not. If the original has a `jtbl_` reference, use a switch, and vice versa.
- id: i-fcross-jumping-merges-identical | keywords: switch, exit, volatile, case, merge, duplicate
  -fcross-jumping merges identical call tails: if retail has the call twice, duplicate it in the source. **But duplicating the arm is not enough when retail's two blocks are byte-identical**: cc1 2.6.0 still merges them, and the merge rule is the INSTRUCTION SHAPE, not the memory flags. `battle/misc20` func_8009E788 has `case 0:` and `case 15:` both `idx = g_BattleTargetSlotState->extraWeaponIds[0]; break;` as separate blocks in retail (`.L8009E7C0` and `.L8009E814`, both `lui/lw/nop/lbu v1,4/j/nop`); the plain body merges them (200 vs retail's 224 bytes, 89.11%). Three spellings measured with `cc1_probe.sh` on a self-contained copy all still merge (one block, both case labels pointing at it, the copy placed at the LATER case's source position): (a) the plain duplicate arm; (b) routing case 15's load through a `u8` temp local (`w = p->e[0]; idx = w;` - the QI copy is folded away before the merge pass); (c) making case 15's POINTER load volatile (`(*(u8 *volatile *)&g) [4]`), i.e. overturning the 'flags differ so rtx differ' guess. Only a case arm that costs a different NUMBER of instructions keeps both blocks: `idx = *(volatile u8 *)&p->e[0];` does, because the un-fusable volatile QI load costs an extra `andi v1,v0,0xff` (224 bytes at retail's size, 98.66%, andi where retail has `lbu v1,4`+`nop`). So when a park's residual is 'retail has two identical arms and mine has one', no flag-level spelling will help - either find the source-level statement that costs an instruction there, or park it. **The merge is also NOT switchable in gcc 2.6.0** (round 417 re-check): its cc1 rejects `-fno-cross-jumping` outright ("Invalid option"), so a 2.6.0 unit cannot opt out for one function; `gcc-2.7.2` scores the same body 85.41 and `gcc-2.7.2-cdk` 66.39, so a preset switch is no answer either. Writing the two arms in retail's own block order (case 0, group, case 15, group, returns) reproduces retail's layout exactly at 200 bytes / 89.11 percent, which is the best an honest body reaches. One more spelling ruled out (round 402, live body): `u8 idx` instead of `s32 idx` with the volatile
  load un-parks 224 -> 208 bytes / 84.29, because a QI local makes the OTHER arm's `extraWeaponIds[3]`
  load gain an `andi v1,v0,0xff` too; the 224-byte volatile spelling needs the s32 index local. And the
  pseudo-register hope (two source variables so jump.c sees rtx-unequal blocks before allocation) is dead:
  cse records the two variables equal and folds the copy before jump.c runs (measured 200 bytes).
- id: i-qi-narrowing-causes-the-cross-jump | keywords: exit, merge, delay, andi, u8, cross-jump, return
  **A LOCAL'S WIDTH DECIDES WHETHER TWO IDENTICAL `return 0` BLOCKS CROSS-JUMP, and QI NARROWING IS WHAT TRIGGERS THE MERGE** (menu/main/misc10 func_801E31C0, parked 99.75 at retail's exact 1020 bytes). Retail's tail is `beqz v1,EXIT / move v0,zero / j EXIT / move v0,s2` (the deep arm's 0 in the beqz delay slot, mpFull in the j's) plus a second `move v0,zero` falling into the epilogue; with the flag declared `u8` (a QI-mode compare, giving retail's `andi v1,s1,0xff`) cc1 2.6.0 cross-jumps the deep arm's 0 onto that second block and inverts the branch (`beqz v0,<zero> / move v0,s2 / j / nop`, 99.69). Declaring the flag `unsigned int` - a permuter find, base 216 -> 205 - stops the merge and gives retail's exact tail, but drops the `andi` (a `nop` fills the slot): 99.75, same size. Every spelling that re-narrows the value brings the merge back - `(flag & 0xFF) == 0` gives the andi and re-merges (99.69), `(u8)flag` in the test and a `u8 copy = flag;` both measure 98.67 / 1012 with the copy replacing the andi, and last-arm `if (...) return v; return 0;` / `else if` chains measure 98.18 / 1004 because the value copies merge too. So when retail materialises the same constant in two places and yours branches to one copy, first check the WIDTH of the variable being tested: QI/HI uses add the `andi` that costs the merge, and the merge is what moves the constant out of the delay slot. Round 405 re-measured it from both sides and the wall is a `jump.c` redirect, not a source shape: the merge fires for EVERY spelling whose 0x4000 test operand is a masked temp - `(flag & 0xFF) == 0`, `(flag & 0xFF) ? v : 0`, `(u8)flag`, and a QI-to-SI copy (`u8 flag; unsigned int flagW; flagW = flag;`) all measure 99.69 at retail's exact 1020 bytes - while any spelling with retail's tail (an SI test on the variable itself) has no `andi`. Two 3-minute permuter runs, one from each side, returned NONE at base 205 and a 124-scoring candidate from the masked body whose only change (routing the deep arm through a local) measures 99.51 / 1024, one instruction MORE than retail. So do not reopen this park without a compiler-level lever: it needs the `andi` and the un-redirected block at once. Round 418 closed the compiler side: `try_flags` scores 91.96 (gcc-2.7.2), 91.3 (2.7.2-cdk), 99.75 (-O3) and 99.75 (-G0), so 2.6.0 with the unit's flags is the only compiler near it; and the redirect is NOT `flag_thread_jumps` - `cc1 -O2 -fno-thread-jumps` emits a `.s` identical to the `-O2` one (diff the two pre-maspsx files: only the option comment differs), so no preset can turn it off. A third 3-minute permuter run from the SI body: NONE, base 205 best 205.
- id: i-a-cross-jumped-block-keeps | keywords: cross-jump, merge, case, block, fallthrough, goto, placement
  **A CROSS-JUMPED BLOCK KEEPS THE POSITION IT WAS EXPANDED AT, SO RETAIL'S COPY SITTING NEXT TO (AND FALLING THROUGH INTO) THE NEXT CASE'S BLOCK IS NOT REACHABLE FROM SOURCE ORDER** (round 397, battling/main/misc3 func_80077A9C, re-parked 97.90% at retail's exact 1720 bytes). Two switch arms each contain `if (pEntry[0] == 0) { p->actionWaitTimer = 0xF; BattleResetMessageQueue(p); }`; cc1 2.7.2 does merge the two copies into one (correct), but it emits it between the case-1 and case-2 entries and ends it with `j <case 0's block>` (3 instructions), where retail's single copy sits after the shared arm body and FALLS THROUGH into case 0's `jal BattleResetMessageQueue` (2 instructions). Two spellings measured: sharing the block explicitly with case 0 through a label (`p->actionWaitTimer = 0xF; goto resetQueue;` in both arms plus `case 0: resetQueue: BattleResetMessageQueue(p);`) gives 94.72% / 1764 bytes - the block still lands at case 1's source position, now with an inverted branch AND the `j`, so the goto does not move it either; and listing the body first (`if (pEntry[0] != 0) {body} else {reset}`, which would put the reset after the body) gives 95.52% / 1760 bytes because the cross-jump stops firing altogether. Case listing order is already retail's 1,2,0,3,4,5 (other orders measure 92.52 / 97.74). So when a park's residual is "same instruction multiset, one merged block in the wrong place", check first whether retail's copy falls through into a neighbouring block: if it does, gcc's expansion order cannot produce it and the park is a BLOCKED placement, not a spelling.

  **SUPERSEDED IN PART (round 421, same function): the "BLOCKED placement, not a spelling" verdict was wrong about HOW to reach it.** Retail's case-1 and case-2 arms share one body (case 1 jumps to the merged `jal BattleActorSelectActionEntry` with `li a1,1` in the jump's delay slot, case 2 falls in with `move a1,zero`), so writing the two duplicated arms as a REAL shared block - each arm `pEntry = BattleActorSelectActionEntry(p, N); goto actionEntry;` and a single `actionEntry:` label between the last arm and the next case, holding the `if (!(p->state & 2)) { if (pEntry[0] != 0) {body} else {reset} }` - makes gcc emit the body at the label's source position, i.e. AFTER case 2 and before case 0, and the reset block then falls through into case 0's `jal BattleResetMessageQueue` exactly as retail has it. Measured 97.90 -> 99.49% at retail's exact 1720 bytes / 430 insns with the whole body instruction-identical. Two load-bearing details: the body-first polarity is required inside the shared block (`== 0` puts the reset before the body and costs a `j`, 1724 bytes / 98.19%), and the duplicate-arm spelling CANNOT work because cross-jump leaves the merged body at the second arm's position while the reset block stays at the first arm's. So before parking a "merged block in the wrong place" switch residual, check whether retail's two arms share a `jal` too: if they do, the shared body is source-level in retail and a `goto` label reproduces the block order.

- id: i-trailing-table-word-null-carve | keywords: jtbl, rodata, pad, carve, check_bin, tile, align
  **A UNIT'S LAST JUMP TABLE CAN CARRY A TRAILING ZERO WORD THAT NO `switch` EMITS, AND A SPLAT `rodata` CARVE AT THAT OFFSET SUPPLIES IT - so this shape of the "4 mod 8 / trailing pad" family is NOT a park** (round 401, menu/main/misc10 func_801E3ECC, 169 insns, XenoMenu260).
