The body was 99.94% at retail's exact 676 bytes / 0x2A4 with the `jtbl_801C522C` vs `.rodata` operand as the only diff, i.e. an ordinary MATCH-JTBL candidate, and `check_bin.sh menu` then failed by 4 bytes (build/out/menu.bin 153860 against disc's 153864) with 130466 differing bytes over 396 symbols starting at byte 0 of the overlay. That is the signature of a rodata size change, not a code change: `.menu` puts every unit's `.rodata` in subsegment order and `menu_TEXT_START` follows it, so 4 bytes lost in misc10's rodata moves EVERY `.text` address down 4 and rewrites every code pointer in the overlay's rodata (disc 0x801C5448 vs build 0x801C5444 at offset 0). Cause: retail's `jtbl_801C522C` is EIGHT words - the seven case entries plus `00000000` at 0x248 - while a seven-case `switch` emits seven, and misc10's range 0x18C..0x24C tiles as 0x28/0x28/0x28/0x28/0x20. Measured non-causes: the tables sit at section offsets 0/0x28/0x50/0x78/0xA0 (all 8-aligned), so no `.align 3` pad is missing or added anywhere, and the three matched neighbours that own C tables are unaffected. FIX, one line in the yaml and no C change: add `- [0x248, rodata]` between `- [0x18C, .rodata, main/misc10]` and `- [0x24C, .rodata, main/misc11]`; gears then writes a 4-byte asm rodata object for it and lists it between `misc10.c.o(.rodata)` and `misc11.c.o(.rodata)` in `linker/menu.ld`, the unit tiles to 0x24C again, and `check_bin.sh menu` prints OK with the C body live (verified both ways in round 401: OK with the carve, MISMATCH without). Apply the test arithmetically before parking anything in this family - if the unit's LAST table ends 4 bytes before the next subsegment boundary, carve the gap and the switch converts; the carve only works at a unit BOUNDARY, so a pad BETWEEN two 4-mod-8 tables inside one range (i-jtbl-midchain-4mod8) still blocks.

- id: i-jtbl-offset-follows-file-position | keywords: jtbl, rodata, align, family, chain, order, include_asm, check_bin
  **A C SWITCH TABLE IS EMITTED AT ITS FUNCTION'S POSITION IN THE .c FILE, INTERLEAVED WITH THE .rodata OF THE `INCLUDE_ASM` STUBS AROUND IT - so converting an in-family stub IN ADDRESS ORDER keeps retail's table offsets and does NOT need the whole family converted in one go** (round 403, battle/main/misc11_b func_80081318/80081504/800816F8, all three 99.90-99.92% at retail's exact byte counts and `check_bin.sh battle` prints OK). The unit's `.rodata` range starts 8-aligned (0x80070010) and holds eight 0x20-byte tables: five owned by asm stubs (0x520, 0x540, 0x560, 0x580, 0x5A0) and two by already-matched C functions (0x5C0, 0x5E0). Converting the SECOND table's stub moves that table from .rodata+0x20 to the position of its C definition, which is still between the first stub's asm table and the third's, so the offsets come out 0x20/0x40/0x60 unchanged; the trap in the "convert the WHOLE switch family in address order" rule is only the case where the converted function's definition is NOT in address order (the older parks here had all their C defs at the END of the file, which is what put their tables after every asm table and produced the +4 pad measurements). Rule of thumb: keep the definition where the stub was, re-run `check_bin.sh <bin>` (objdiff cannot see this), and ignore park notes that call this JTBL_LAYOUT - the three parks this closed had blamed the section alignment for four rounds when the residual was only the `jtbl_X` vs `.rodata` operand. Reading the result: the object's `.rodata` grows by 0x20 per converted switch in report mode, and only matching mode tells you the interleaved order.

- id: i-equal-field-return-cannot | keywords: exit, return, fold, beq, move, epilogue, prevFrame, polarity
  **`if (frame == p->field) return p->field;` CANNOT KEEP THE FIELD'S LOAD IN `$v0` FOR THE EPILOGUE - gcc records the equality on the branch edge and returns the register it compared, so that exit costs one instruction however it is spelled** (round 404, battle/main/misc22 func_800A3E98, parked 90.65% / 1040 bytes against retail's 1028, and its lib_gear twin func_801E1258 88.98%). Retail is `lhu v0,0x18(s0) / nop / beq s1,v0,<epilogue> / nop`, i.e. the value handed back is the loaded field and the taken edge does no work at all. The early-return form compiles to `bne s1,v0,<body> / li ... / j <epilogue> / move v0,s1`; moving the early return OUT and wrapping the body (`if (frame != prevFrame) { ...; return (s16)level; } return prevFrame;`) does produce retail's `beq s1,v0,<epilogue>` directly - with the mode's `li v0,0x1` even landing in the delay slot - but the equal path still needs its own `move v0,s1`, that block lands after the truncating exit, and the truncating exit then needs a `j`: +2 at the tail instead of +1 at the branch (90.65 vs 90.63). Measured dead on the returned expression: the field, a u16 local, an s32 local, a u32 local of it, and both compare operand orders. Use the shared-exit restructure for the branch POLARITY and count the tail block as its price; do not expect the fold to go away.
- id: i-a-shared-repair-block-goes | keywords: if-else, chain, block, order, goto, label, merge, branch, exit
  **A RETRY/REPAIR BLOCK THAT SEVERAL FAILURE TESTS SHARE SITS *BEFORE* THE REMAINING TESTS IN RETAIL, AND THE ONLY SPELLING THAT PUTS IT THERE IS A LABEL INSIDE THE FIRST ARM'S Then-BODY THAT THE OTHER ARMS `goto`** (field/main/misc4 func_8007BAC0, round 406, gcc 2.7.2 Default, parked 99.22% at retail's exact 269 insns / 1076 bytes). Retail's shape after the probe is `slt / beqz v0,L_FLAGS / addiu a0,sp,0x50` (the fixup falls through the first test) with `L_FLAGS`, the 0x420000 arm and the 0x40 test laid out AFTER the fixup block and each `b`/`j`ing back to it. Measured on one body: the plain `if (A || B || (C && D) || E) {fixup} else {restore}` OR-chain compiles the tests first, fixup last - 81.90%; `if (A) { retry: fixup } else if (B) goto retry; else if (C) { if (D) goto retry; } else if (E) goto retry; else restore` (a label at the head of the first then-block) reproduces retail's block order and every branch target - 94.15%; duplicating the restore block into two arms so cross-jumping merges it is WORSE (260 insns / 1040 bytes / 93.93), so do not duplicate the merged block, duplicate nothing and use the label.
  Two more measured facts on the same body: (1) an arm whose test feeds the shared block must branch on its FALSE case (`if ((mat & K) == 0) goto restore; goto retry;`) - with the true case spelled `if (...) goto retry;` cc1 merges that branch with the next arm's into one `j` + shared compare and costs 3 instructions (94.15 vs 98.30, 1068 vs 1076 bytes); (2) an inert `if (ptr) {copy} else {copy}` around the merged block is LOAD-BEARING for the reloads retail has (a post-reload cross-jump gives the merged block a copy's allocation, so retail's two extra reloads appear) - worth 98.30 -> 99.22 at retail's exact byte count, but say so in the park: it is a spelling no one would ship. Residual after all of that is ONE displaced load (`lw a2,0x24(s2)` two slots early in retail) and two permuter runs (1629, 1775 iterations) found nothing.
  **CLOSED in round 419: that 99.22 body was WRONG CODE, and the two claims above about the `if (ptr)` duplication are stale.** The successful-retry arm, written as the last statement of the first test's then-block, FALLS OUT of the if/else chain into the restore block, so it put the saved probe values back on the path retail jumps past; objdiff showed it as the only remaining unexplained row, `j <func+0x388>` against retail's `j <func+0x3ba>`-style `func+0x3b8` - and a `j` whose addend differs from retail's is a REAL control-flow difference (unlike the branch rows, whose printed addresses are per-object), i.e. the label landed exactly on the restore block's first instruction. Spelling the retry as `pActor->scriptFlags.flags |= K; goto storeback;` plus a `storeback:` label at the store-back block gives retail's `j` target and 99.24% at the same 269 insns, and THEN the inert `if (pVec) {copy} else {copy}` is no longer needed at all: the plain six-assignment restore measures 100.00% / 1076 bytes, so delete it rather than shipping a spelling no one would type. The last displaced load closes by writing the retry's direction components in retail's evaluation order, `dir.vx = -v.vx >> 8; dir.vy = ((out.vy << 16) - pActor->position.vy) >> 8; dir.vz = -v.vz >> 8;` (vy between vx and vz), which issues `lw a2,0x24(s2)` two slots earlier into `a2` - 100.00%, and `check_bin.sh field` prints OK. Lesson beyond this function: when a park explains its own size by an inert construct, check the control flow first - a wrong code path can own the bytes a spelling was credited with.

- id: i-a-shared-jal-tail-is | keywords: jal, tail, merge, cross-jump, epilogue, fall-through, block, order
  **A SHARED `jal` TAIL THAT SEVERAL ARMS JUMP INTO IS NOT CROSS-JUMPING THAT gcc LOSES - 2.7.2 merges identical call tails by itself, and what it needs is every arm ENDING AT THE SAME EPILOGUE, i.e. the success arm written as an early `return` and the error handler out of line** (round 412, world_map/main/misc15 func_80096C0C, 64.15 -> 100.00 at retail's exact 780 bytes / 195 insns, XenoWorldMap preset). The park blamed the layout plus "the single shared `jal CdControlF` that retail cross-jumps the three call sites into, for 5 extra instructions", and factored the command into a `cmd`/`pParam` variable to hunt the merge. Both were wrong: the unit's already-matched neighbour WorldMapCdReadCallback (same file, retail .s in `asm/<bin>/matchings/`) has the identical `jal CdControlF` tail with four entries and a constant per arm (`li a0,1` / `li a0,9` / `li a0,0x13` / `li a0,2`) written as SEPARATE calls, so the merge is ordinary tail sharing of `jal X; nop` followed by the same block. What produced it here: `if (mode == 1) { CdGetSector(...); if (sector == expected) { ...; g_ExpectedSector++; return; } <error handler A> } else { <error handler B> }` - the POSITIVE test on the success condition puts the success chain in the fall-through, both error copies end at the function's last block (the `CdControlF` call), and the `return` at the end of the success chain is what keeps the `expected++` block shared by its other four entries. Cost of not doing it: 800 bytes against retail's 780, and the top test coming out `beq` into the success chain. Two more residuals from the same match: a `u_char` FIRST PARAMETER emits `andi a0,a0,0xff` before the `li`/`bne` (retail has none -> the parameter is `s32`, and its `CdlCB` callback typedef then needs an incompatible-pointer warning tolerated or a cast at the `CdReadyCallback` call site), and a local reused for two different values (`sector` for the head sector and again for the next chunk's) forces ONE register where retail keeps `a0`/`a1` - two separate locals (`sector`, `nextSector`) is what restores both.

- id: i-jtbl-keeps-its-own-offset | keywords: jtbl, rodata, family, offset, include_asm, world_map, align, check_bin
  **CONFIRMED IN A SECOND BIN, FROM THE OTHER DIRECTION: converting ONE stub of an asm-owned table family leaves that table at retail's offset, so a park note that says "the whole switch family has to be converted in address order" is stale - the only tables C cannot produce are the 4-mod-8 ones, and `.align 3` is what blocks those** (round 414, world_map/main/misc12 func_8008E4F4, 99.80% at retail's exact 396 bytes / 99 insns, XenoWorldMap preset). The unit's `.rodata` is 0x258 bytes of nine tables starting 8-aligned at 0x800709F8, with E190's two at +0x00/+0x18 and this one's at +0x38 all owned by `INCLUDE_ASM` stubs; converting E4F4 alone (its definition is in address order, where its stub was) kept its table at +0x38 and `check_bin.sh world_map` printed OK, with `cmp build/out/world_map.bin disc/world_map.bin` byte-identical - the C table was assembled after the earlier stub's asm tables because it is emitted at its own function's place in the file. The residual objdiff cannot fold here is the table operand (`jtbl_80070A30` against `.rodata`) plus `addiu $s0,$s2,%lo(D_1F8000A0)`, which this body writes as the folded literal 0xA0 off the same base; naming the symbol to get the reloc costs the shared base register (388 bytes, 93.12), so 99.80 is this function's floor and MATCH-level. Companion: the unit's five tables at and after +0x15C are all 4 mod 8 and stay unconvertible, and the same round corrected the world_map/misc12 park notes that had called the whole prefix JTBL_LAYOUT.

- id: i-case-body-order-is-source-order | keywords: switch, case, block, order, arm, jump table, dispatch
  **RETAIL'S CASE BLOCKS ARE EMITTED IN THE ORDER THE `case` LABELS APPEAR IN THE SOURCE, SO A SWITCH WHOSE ARMS ARE NORMALISED INTO NUMERIC ORDER CAN NEVER MATCH A RETAIL DISPATCH THAT IS SCRAMBLED - reordering the labels alone took a body from 6.21% to 69.42%** (round 415, battle/main/misc30 func_800B9508, parked 72.11%, XenoBattleTailCdk0). Retail's 13-entry table (0xF3..0xFF) has its bodies laid out F3,F4,F5,F6,F7,F8,FA / F9 / FF / FE / FD / FC / FB / default, i.e. descending 0xFF->0xFB after the group, with the 0xFB arm LAST - so the source's `case` labels were in that order and every arm had to be moved to match. The table itself is emitted in case-VALUE order and is unaffected, which is why the dispatch head and the table were already exact while the whole body after the `jr` was shifted; a `diff_func` run on such a body prints hundreds of differing lines and reads like a broken structure rather than a reordering. Read the arm order out of the original `.s` (walk the `jtbl_` entries to their labels, then note the code offsets, not the case values) before writing a switch with more than three arms in a cdk/-G0 battle unit.

- id: i-a-last-table-takes-the-unit | keywords: jtbl, rodata, pad, range, carve, yaml, culprit, check_bin
  **CONVERTING THE UNIT'S *LAST* TABLE KEEPS RETAIL'S OFFSET BUT DROPS THE ASM STUB'S TRAILING PAD WORD, SO THE UNIT'S `.rodata` COMES UP 4 BYTES SHORT OF ITS YAML RANGE AND EVERY SYMBOL AFTER IT IN THE OVERLAY SHIFTS - the fix is a 4-byte `rodata` carve before the next unit's range, not a different C** (round 419, menu/main/misc9 func_801E05D0, parked 98.88% at 2468 bytes against retail's 2472). The earlier table levers (`i-jtbl-offset-follows-file-position`, `i-jtbl-keeps-its-own-offset`, `i-jtbl-misc5-bbec-converts-alone`) all converted a table that had an asm sibling AFTER it, whose own 4-byte pad kept the range's total size; here the converted function is the last of the three, so nothing replaces the pad. Measured, unparked and built in matching mode: the object's `.rodata` is 0x8C with the two remaining asm tables at +0x00/+0x30 and the C table at +0x60 - retail's exact address, because cc1 emits function_rodata after its function's body (the `.align 3` table is the last thing in `build/src/<unit>.c.s`, after the earlier stubs' `.include`d `.rodata`) - and `culprit.sh menu` then lists the whole overlay as differing, which is the 4-byte shift, not the functions. `config/menu.yaml` already carries the precedent for this exact situation one unit later (`[0x248, rodata]` after main/misc10, with a comment naming jtbl_801C522C's unemitted trailing zero word). Read a `culprit.sh` run that flags dozens of unrelated functions together with `check_bin.sh`'s byte counts as a layout shortfall, not as a bad match.

- id: i-compare-tree-switch-is-recoverable-from-the-lui-ori-pairs | keywords: switch, dispatch, compare, tree, lui, ori, arm order, case values, sparse
  **A SPARSE 32-bit SWITCH EMITS A BINARY COMPARE TREE, NOT A TABLE, AND BOTH THE CASE VALUES AND THE ARM ORDER CAN BE READ STRAIGHT OUT OF IT: each node is a `lui $v0,hi` / `ori $v0,$v0,lo` pair (the `.s` writes the FULL constant inside both the `>> 16` and the `& 0xFFFF` expressions, so mask them yourself) followed by `beq $v1,$v0,TARGET`, `sltu $v0,$v0,$v1` and `bnez` - collect (constant,target) and group by target to get each arm's case labels, and take the arms' CODE OFFSET order as the source order** (round 423, slus_006.64/psyq/libgpu_c func_800477D0, 1208 insns / 0x12E0 bytes, matched 100.00% on the first scored run, PSY-Q preset). Two parsing traps: a block's `ori` at a branch target continues a `lui` that was hoisted into the PREDECESSOR's delay slot (so a linear scan attributes the wrong high half - the same low half always appears twice, once in the fallthrough path where the `lui` is explicit), and the mask constant's own `lui/ori` sits in the last `beq`'s delay slot on the way to the default arm. Here the 16 arms were 8 primitive families x {L, non-L}: code order was F3L,G3L,FT3L,GT3L,F3,G3,FT3,GT3,F4L,G4L,FT4L,GT4L,F4,G4,FT4,GT4 while the case values are sorted by the id word, so writing the labels in value order emits 16 blocks in the wrong place. Companion lever from the same function: the arm bodies are a per-primitive-type copy of a packet parameter block whose fields overlap at DIFFERENT widths (`lbu` at 0x8 in one arm, `lhu` at 0x8 in another), so do not try to find one typed source struct - declare the packet as a union of `u_long tag` / `u_char bytes[N]` / `u_short shorts[N/2]` views and index it by packet offset; gcc emits the load modes exactly and the redundancy (the same byte loaded three times) survives because every store to the destination may alias the packet.


- id: i-two-empty-extra-labels-are-what-root-a-case-tree-at-the-middle | keywords: switch, case, tree, branch, empty, default, slti, beq
  **A SMALL SWITCH WHOSE ONLY BODY SITS IN THE LOWEST CASE STILL EMITS RETAIL'S `beq hi / slti hi+1 / bne lo` CHAIN IF THE SOURCE CARRIES TWO EXTRA BODYLESS LABELS - one empty case label merges into the default and leaves a single compare, two of them give cc1 three case nodes, root the balanced tree at the MIDDLE value and produce exactly the retail chain** (round 424, world_map/main/misc7 func_80081470, 81.84 -> 89.94% on the switch edit alone with the byte count and instruction count both landing on retail's 620 / 155, XenoWorldMapDiv; cc1_probe.sh reproduces it in isolation: `switch (x)` with cases {1,2} emits one `bne x,1`, with cases {1,2,3} and empty bodies on 2 and 3 emits `beq x,2` / `slt x,3` / `bne x,1` all targeting the switch exit). So do not read retail's extra compare as a missing second body: count the compare chain, and let the labels' values (2 and 3 here) fix the source's case values.

- id: g-jumptable-span-is-min-to-max-case-not-the-distinct-arms | keywords: switch, jtbl, table, case, span, min, max, tree, case-labels
  **A RETAIL JUMP TABLE WHOSE RANGE CHECK IS WIDER THAN THE DISTINCT ARMS MEANS THE C HAS (EMPTY) CASE LABELS DOWN TO THE TABLE'S MINIMUM: gcc spans min..max over ALL case values and fills the rest with the default label, and with only the distinct arms it emits a `beq` TREE instead of a table** (measured with `cc1_probe.sh battle/main/misc34`, gcc-2.7.2-cdk -G0, round 428). battle/main/misc34 func_800BFE48 has `addiu v0,s0,-5 / sltiu v0,11` and an 11-word table in BOTH bit-walk loops, but only bits 13/14/15 have distinct bodies (8 of the 11 entries are the loop-continue): `switch (bit) { case 13/14/15: ... }` compiles to three `beq`s and no table at all, while adding `case 5: case 6: case 7: case 8: case 9: case 10: case 11: case 12: break;` reproduces retail's `addu $2,$16,-5 / sltu $2,$2,11`, the `li -20` table-base bias and the 11-word table exactly. Same reading on the same function's first switch, where the table spans 5..21 for the five real arms (5/7/14/15/21). So count the table words against the arms BEFORE rewriting the arms: the surplus entries are case labels in the source, not padding.
- id: g-switch-index-narrow-type-adds-the-sll-sra-range-check | keywords: switch, jtbl, sll, sra, andi, index, narrow, char, sign
  **A RANGE CHECK THAT SIGN-EXTENDS THE SUBTRACTED INDEX (`addiu v0,v0,-5 / sll v0,v0,24 / sra v1,v0,24 / sltiu`) MEANS THE SWITCH INDEX IS A SIGNED CHAR EXPRESSION: `switch ((s8)field)` gives exactly that, a bare `u8` field or a `u8` local gives the same three instructions WITHOUT the sll/sra pair** (measured with `cc1_probe.sh battle/main/misc34`, round 428, battle/main/misc34 func_800BFE48: the `(s8)` cast was worth 85.29 -> 85.96% and one instruction). The load stays `lbu` in both, so the sign extension in the range test - not the load opcode - is the tell that the source cast the byte to `s8` at the switch.

- id: g-symmetric-switch-arms-cross-jump | keywords: switch, arm, cross-jump, tail, merge, do-while, preheader, hoist
  **SIX SWITCH ARMS THAT EACH END IN THE SAME STORE QUADRUPLE GET THEIR TAILS CROSS-JUMPED INTO ONE SHARED BLOCK, WHICH RETAIL DOES NOT HAVE: an empty `do { } while (0)` after the quadruple stops the merge but makes loop.c hoist the refill load into its preheader, out of retail's order** (round 429, world_map/main/misc8 func_80083A00, 377 insns, XenoWorldMapDiv; parked, best 69.79 percent / 347 insns against retail's 395, and the same body without the do-block is 60.45 / 351 with the tails merged). Both halves are measurable in one diff each: plain arms, or arms whose tails were made non-identical by moving a store into the refill, merge or scatter (60.45, 58.78); with the do-block the quadruple stays duplicated but the `lh` that refills the just-written halfword rises above the two int stores through the actor pointer (gcc 2.7.2 has type-based alias sets only, so a short load crosses an int store freely) and the two constant stores sink below it. Neither spelling reaches retail's order AND block count. Companion fact from the same function, which is the reusable half: a view-angle refill must be a VOLATILE STORE with a plain read (`*(volatile s16 *)&g_WorldMapViewRotationY = K;` then `... << 12`); a volatile READ instead moves every store in the arm and costs the whole case, and pinning the neighbouring stores volatile to hold the order pays less than it costs (67.08 / 366, 66.74 / 345).

- id: g-case-arms-are-emitted-in-source-order-not-case-value-order | keywords: switch, case, arm, order, block, layout, goto, tail, source-order
  **A SWITCH'S ARM BODIES ARE EMITTED IN SOURCE ORDER, SO WHEN RETAIL'S ARMS ARE NOT IN CASE-VALUE ORDER THE C MUST LIST THEM IN RETAIL'S PHYSICAL ORDER - writing the natural 1..N order leaves every arm 4-8 bytes off and objdiff collapses 89.47% -> 76.16% on a byte-for-byte identical instruction multiset** (round 430, battling/main/misc1 func_8007107C, a 426-insn battle-script interpreter with a 35-word table, Default preset, parked 89.47% / 1684 bytes; read retail's arm order off the `.s` addresses and its case values off the table). Retail's 34 arms sit at .L80071120.. in the order 1,2,3,4,5,6,**8,7,10,9**,11,12,13,**15,14**,**20,18,19,27**,21,22,... - i.e. three runs where a later opcode's body comes first - and since a `case` label's value does not depend on its position, listing the blocks in that order costs nothing and is the whole 13 points. Two companion facts measured on the same body: (a) `case 0: return;` must exist - retail's guard is `sltiu v1,0x23` with table entry 0 pointing at the epilogue, so opcode 0 ends the script and only the OUT-of-range value is the default; without it cc1 biases the index (`addiu v1,v0,-1 / sltiu v1,34`) and the table moves; (b) the shared tails retail has (one `armWait` block reached from four arms, one `turn` block from four more, one `cursor++`/`cursor+=2` latch) ARE reproduced by `goto` labels inside the `for (;;)` - cc1 2.7.2 does merge those - so do not duplicate the tails to "look natural" and do not conclude the arms are unmergeable; the leftovers on that park are one private `li <angle>` + tail copy per constant-angle arm instead of retail's single block entered with the angle in `$v0`, plus the `sll`-in-the-beqz-delay-slot insn (`c-stolen-delay-slot-retail` class).

- id: a-gcc-puts-an-if-then-block-last-and-a-switch-default-where-its-label-is | keywords: switch, case, default, block, order, if, goto, layout
  **gcc 2.7.2 EMITS `if (c) A; B;` WITH THE BRANCH TO A AND A PLACED LAST, AND A SWITCH'S `default:` BODY EXACTLY WHERE THE LABEL SITS - so retail's order [cases][rotation path][case-0 path][shared tail] means the case-0 body was NOT the switch's default arm but a label after the rotation code, which only a `goto` shape reproduces** (round 431, field/main/misc8 func_80083288, 78.75 -> 83.18, 448 insns / 1792 bytes vs retail's 451 / 1804). Probed first: a plain `switch (x & 3) { case 1..3 default }` puts the default body BEFORE the code that follows the switch (the dispatch chain is retail's, so the switch form is right and only the body order was wrong). Writing `case 3:` to break into a `rotate:` label, `default: goto parent;` and `parent:` + a following `setMatrices:` label reproduces retail's layout, and the threaded-away `goto parent` is exactly what makes retail's compare chain end in `j <case-0 block>`. The mirror lever on the tail: retail's `beq` onto the `-1` block means the source is `if (minHeight == NONE) { free; return -1; } ...return 0;` - the negated `if` emits `bne` and puts the two blocks the other way round (74.01 -> 75.91).
- id: g-case-label-that-does-nothing-sets-the-table-span | keywords: switch, jtbl, sltiu, table, span, case, empty, fallthrough
  **A RETAIL TABLE WIDER THAN THE HIGHEST DISTINCT ARM MEANS THE SOURCE CARRIES AN EMPTY `case <max>: break;`, AND AN ARM WHOSE `state++` RETAIL JUMPS TO A SHARED STORE MEANS THAT ARM DOES NOT FALL INTO ITS NEIGHBOUR** (round 431, world_map/main/misc11 func_8008D678, 445 insns, XenoWorldMap preset, parked 99.96% on a table-alignment pad only). Two measured halves, both visible in one diff each: (a) the state switch's highest distinct body is case 50, so the natural spelling gives `sltiu v1,51` and a 51-word table where retail tests `sltiu v1,65` - state 0x40 is a live case whose body IS the switch exit, so `case 64: break;` restores the 65-word table (14 words / 56 bytes, and every later table in the unit with it); (b) retail's `lhu v0,0x20(s1) / j <shared`sh v0,0x20(s1)`> / addiu v0,v0,1` is the arm BREAKING, not falling through, while the arm above it keeps the store inline - so copy the twin's `/* falls through */` only where retail's next block is the neighbour's body. The one-line check is to count `sh v0,0x20(s1)` in both objects (9 vs 8 here) instead of reading it as a cross-jump residue; that single statement was the last 4 bytes of the function.
- id: g-owned-jtbl-at-4-mod-8-can-never-be-emitted | keywords: jtbl, align, pad, layout, rodata, shift, overlay, table
  **A FUNCTION'S SWITCH TABLES ARE `.align 3`-PADDED RELATIVE TO THE UNIT'S `.rodata` RANGE START, SO TABLES THAT RETAIL PACKS AT 4 MOD 8 COST A 4-BYTE PAD AND SHIFT THE WHOLE OVERLAY - measure the offset from the range start before blaming the body** (round 431, world_map/main/misc11 func_8008D678: the body is retail's instruction-for-instruction at retail's exact 1780 bytes / 445 insns and objdiff 99.96% - only the two `lw v0,<jtbl>` operands differ - yet `check_bin.sh world_map` reports the overlay 4 bytes long). The two tables (jtbl_800708D4, jtbl_800708F4) sit 0x3BC and 0x3DC bytes into the range - base 0x800708D4-0x3BC = 0x80070518, which is 0xA28 + 0x8006FAF0, i.e. the yaml's `[0xA28, .rodata, main/misc11]` - and BOTH are 4 mod 8. Proof it is the pad and not the body: with the stub parked (tables supplied by the asm stub, splat places them exactly) the object's `.rodata` is 0x4E0 = the yaml range and `check_bin.sh world_map` is OK; with the C live cc1's `.align 3` at offset 0x3BC pads to 0x3C0, the section comes out 0x4E4, every later byte of the overlay is +4 and the binary is 4 bytes long. Read it off with `mips-linux-gnu-objdump -h -r -j .rodata build/src/<unit>.c.o` (the last relocation run is the function's tables, and its start offset is the one to compare against `% 8`). Unlike i-jtbl-offset-follows-file-position the file position here is already right (the C definition is in address order), and unlike i-trailing-table-word-null-carve the 4 bytes are a pad BEFORE the tables, so neither the position nor a splat carve at the unit's end can move it: park it and say JTBL_LAYOUT. Third such unit after world_map/misc12 and menu/misc10.
- id: g-parity-4-split-is-the-route-for-a-4-mod-8-tail-table | keywords: jtbl, align, pad, parity, split, rodata, yaml, check_bin
  **THE FIX FOR THE `g-owned-jtbl-at-4-mod-8` CLASS IS A SPLIT THAT GIVES THE TAIL ITS OWN `.rodata` RANGE OF PARITY 4, AND THE ARITHMETIC THAT SAYS WHETHER IT WORKS IS `(retail_table_file_offset % 8)`: the new object's section start supplies that parity, so every table the new file emits lands at `start + 8k` and the pad disappears** (round 444, world_map/main/misc11 func_8008D678, re-confirmed with a direct byte comparison: `cmp` shows the FIRST differing byte of `build/out/world_map.bin` is file 0xDE4 - four zero pad bytes exactly where jtbl_800708D4 belongs - and every later word that holds a `.text` address, including misc1's rodata words at file 0x4, reads +4; rodata is this overlay's first section, so one pad in a late unit shifts the whole file). The recipe for that function, in case a later round wants a 445-insn match for two yaml lines: misc11's range is `[0xA28, .rodata, main/misc11]` and the tables are its last ones at 0xDE4/0xE04 (both 4 mod 8 from 0xA28, which is 0 mod 8), so cut the unit at the function's file offset - `[0xA28, .rodata, main/misc11]` + `[0xDE4, .rodata, main/misc11_b]` and `[0x1AC3C, c, main/misc11]` + `[0x1DB88, c, main/misc11_b]`, moving func_8008D678 and the stubs after it into `src/world_map/main/misc11_b.c` (plus a `paths` key for the new file, never a directory key). The new object's `.rodata` then starts at file 0xDE4 (parity 4), the two C tables sit at internal 0 and 0x20 with no pad, and the tail rodata 0xEA8..0xF08 rides along as asm from the moved stubs. Same shape as the misc12 tail split this lever family already recommends; a worker cannot do it (new .c file + yaml + gears.toml), so report SPLIT_JTBL_PARITY4 with the two offsets instead of parking silently. ROUND 452 APPLIED IT: misc11's c range now ends at file 0x1C874 and `[0xCBC, .rodata, main/misc11_c]` starts the tail at parity 4, so misc11_c's first table (jtbl_800707AC) sits at internal +0 - func_8008C364 closed with `check_bin.sh world_map` OK on the first try.

- id: i-a-volatile-lvalue-keeps-a-load-in-the-merged-tail | keywords: switch, case, cross-jump, duplicate, load, volatile, merged, tail
  **WHEN TWO CASE ARMS ARE CROSS-JUMPED AND gcc COPIES THE SHARED TAIL'S GLOBAL LOAD INTO BOTH ARMS, MAKING THAT LOAD A `volatile` LVALUE PINS IT INSIDE THE MERGED BLOCK - it pays one instruction (`lui+addiu+lw` instead of the `lw $r,sym` macro's `lui+lw`) and saves the duplicate copy, so the body gets SHORTER** (round 436, world_map/main/misc6 func_8007EE34, XenoWorldMapDiv preset: 98.17% / 2692 bytes -> 98.42% / 2684, retail 2680). Retail's case 3 arm is only `lw v1,0x38(s3); j; addiu v0,1` and the merged tail owns the `g_WorldMapPlayerPosition` load; our body emitted that load in BOTH arms (2 extra instructions) because the volatile stores to `command`/`state` around it were not enough of a barrier. `*(volatile u32 *)g_WorldMapPlayerPosition` in both arms moved it into the tail, and the merged tail then costs 3 instructions where retail's copy costs 2 - i.e. only worth it when the duplicate exists (count the copies in the diff per case, not per tail). Measured dead on the same body: making the plain x store volatile or not (identical either way), and reading the position into a named local before the two stores (`s32 nDirX = pActor->dirX; nPosX = *(u32 *)g_WorldMapPlayerPosition;`), which gcc folds/schedules back to the same object.

- id: i-default-grouped-with-a-shared-arm-retargets-the-range-check | keywords: switch, default, jtbl, out-of-range, target, beqz, check_bin, park
  **A SWITCH WHOSE TABLE COVERS EVERY VALUE THE INDEX CAN TAKE NEEDS NO `default:` AT ALL; WRITING `default:` ON A SHARED CASE BODY RETARGETS cc1's OUT-OF-RANGE GUARD AT THAT BODY INSTEAD OF AT THE SWITCH EXIT - one branch offset wrong, objdiff still reads 99.87% at retail's exact byte count, and only `check_bin.sh` sees it (one byte: `beqz v0,+7` against retail's `+0x4B`)** (round 452, world_map/main/misc11_c func_8008C364, XenoWorldMap, 460 bytes / 115 insns, closed byte-identical apart from the `jtbl_` operand). The body is a 3-bit init-kind switch whose 8 table entries are cases 0/2/4/6 (one shared `result = 3` block), 1, 3 and 5+7; the round-451 park also wrote `default:` on the `result = 3` label, so cc1's `sltiu a0,8` guard branched into that shared block and a `kind >= 8` would have returned 3 instead of 1 - unreachable at runtime (`kind |= 1|2|4` maxes at 7) and therefore invisible to any behavioural test, but it is a real instruction difference. Deleting the `default:` label makes the guard branch to the switch exit (retail's target) and nothing else moves. So before believing a park that says "the only objdiff delta is `jtbl_` vs `.rodata`", diff the dispatch's out-of-range branch TARGET too: with a fully populated table it must reach the block after the switch, not a case body.

## stack frame size and spills

- id: j-re-fetch-the-table-not-a-cached-pointer | keywords: spill, pointer, cached, re-fetch, table, globals, lbu, lw
  **WHEN RETAIL DOES THE SAME `lbu`-FROM-A-CURSOR PLUS `lw`-FROM-A-TABLE-POINTER LOAD GROUP TWO OR THREE TIMES PER LOOP ITERATION, THE SOURCE NAMED THE TABLE EXPRESSION EVERY TIME - caching the record pointer in a local costs an 8% band and needs a callee-saved register retail never had** (round 430, battle/main/misc16 func_8008D598, 71.88 -> 86.41% at 1664 bytes / 416 insns against retail 1692 / 423, XenoBattle260, 423-instruction body). The diff's tell is retail doing `lbu v0,0(cursor)` + `lw v0,D_TABLE(..)` before the geometry, again after the call for the shade, and a fourth time in the latch for the next record's command word, where a `BattleTileRecord *pRecords = D_TABLE[codes[i]];` body does one fetch and holds the pointer in `$s` across `BattleDrawSpritePackage`. Writing `D_TABLE[codes[i]][r].field` at every use reproduced retail's traffic and moved 71.88 -> 86.41 with the frame, the 10 saved registers and the fade path unchanged; the cached-pointer spelling of the SAME body is 78.35 at 1720 bytes (+28), and re-fetching while ALSO converting the neighbouring `rowOffset` sums to a `pQueue = g_Global + rowOffset` local is 85.15 at 1640 (it hoists the base load, see b-inplace-flips-pointer-base-but-hoists-mem-base). Narrow-type companions measured on the same body: `cmd` must be s32 (u32 gives `sltiu` where retail has `slti`, u16 gives a stray `andi 0xffff` before each compare), `shade` u32 (retail `sltiu ... 0x2000`), `action` u32. The surviving 7-instruction gap is retail's SECOND listIndex biv (it spills pListIndex, pCodesStart, pCodes and pLists at 0x28/0x30/0x38/0x40 and reloads them per use where the index-array body needs two), so the next question for this body is the lever c-pointer-from-memory-paren-sum-is-base-first's `volatile` pointer pair, used as a measurement only.

- id: j-scratchpad-base-first-places-the-prologue | keywords: prologue, order, load, spill, lui, scratchpad, s4, delay-slot, statement
  **ASSIGNING THE SCRATCHPAD BASE POINTER (`pPos = (WorldMapPos*)0x1F800000;`) AS THE FIRST STATEMENT, ABOVE THE INDEX/POINTER CHAIN, IS WHAT PUTS RETAIL'S PROLOGUE BACK - the spill `sw s4,48(sp)/lui s4,0x1F80`, the `g_WorldMapSlots` load above `sll a1,s6,7`, and the branch-delay `addu s2` (round 430, world_map/main/misc9 func_80087FD0, 96.81 -> 99.75 -> 99.92 with the last two rows being splat's `%lo(D_1F800150)` names, check_bin OK). The old park blamed "prologue order ... swapping the first two source assignments changes nothing" - true, but it had the constant pointer LAST, so gcc scheduled its `lui` after the level-index load and the `g_WorldMapSlots` load sank to just before its `addu`, which in turn let the compare take `$v0` and reorg steal the pSlot `addu` into the `beqz` slot (retail's compare is `sltiu v1` BECAUSE `$v0` is still live for the delayed `addu s2`). So when a prologue diff shows a constant-address `lui` two slots late and a `beqz` slot holding a different `addu`, move the constant-base assignment to the top of the body before touching anything else; the same statement moved 36 differing lines to 4. Companion find on the same body: a scratchpad call argument retail encodes as `ori a1,s4,0xA0` needs the source to be a BITWISE OR (`(SVECTOR*)((u32)pPos | 0xA0)`), not `(u8*)pPos + 0xA0`, which gives `addiu` and therefore different bytes (addiu vs ori is an opcode, so unlike a reloc-name residual check_bin sees it).
- id: j-a-uniform-sp-offset-shift-is | keywords: frame, stack, shift, offsets, gap, missing, locals, vars
  **WHEN EVERY `$sp` OFFSET IN THE DIFF IS OFF BY THE SAME CONSTANT THE BODY IS MISSING THAT MANY BYTES OF LOCALS - enumerate retail's own sp-relative offsets, subtract the objects you can name, and the LEFTOVER GAPS are the missing slots, so do not go hunting the residual in the statements** (round 429, battle/main/misc31 func_800BC460, parked 78.90% at 1592 bytes / 398 insns against retail's 1604 / 401, XenoBattleTailCdk0). `grep -oE '0x[0-9A-F]+\(\$sp\)' <stub>.s | sort -u` plus the `addiu $aX,$sp,N` that build the arguments gives retail's whole local map in one look: func_800BC460's objects are total VECTOR 0x10, groupA/groupB SVECTOR 0x20/0x28, frameMatrix MATRIX 0x30, projected VECTOR 0x50, vertex SVECTOR 0x60, an 8-byte SVECTOR 0x68, zeroVec 0x70, two `long` out-params 0x78/0x7C, nearMatrix 0x80, nearScreen 0xA0, nearVec 0xB0, farMatrix 0xB8, farVec 0xE8 - and retail's `.frame` vars are 232 against the body's 208, the 24 bytes being three slots NOTHING in the function reads (4 at 0x6C, 16 at 0xD8, 4 at 0xF4 of tail padding). Two measured consequences worth re-checking on any such park: (1) the 0x68 slot is 8 bytes, not 4, because retail writes `sh` to 0x68/0x6A twice (unscaled, then times 4) - it is an `SVECTOR` whose halves are dashed with `-=`/`*=` and whose radius multiplies use `(s16)(dx*4)` (`sll 18` / `sra 16` truncates `dx*4`, not `dx`), and spelling it that way puts vertex/sxy/zeroVec on retail's exact 0x60/0x68/0x70 while costing 15 instructions to gcc re-storing the addressable struct; (2) two ADDRESSABLE 4-BYTE SCALARS (`long p, flag` passed as `&p`/`&flag`) declared between the SVECTOR and the MATRIX did NOT take the slots after it - they landed at 0xD8/0xDC, above every aggregate, so in this body the 4-byte expand-time scalars came last while all the 8/16/32-byte objects kept declaration order (contrast j-an-expand-time-slot-is-always-the-lowest).

- id: j-cc1-never-reuses-a-stack-slot | keywords: frame, overlap, reuse, share, aliasing, cast, slot, addressable, expand
  **CC1 2.7.2-cdk NEVER REUSES A STACK SLOT, SO TWO LOCALS AT THE SAME `$sp` OFFSET IN RETAIL MEAN THE SOURCE REALLY DOES ALIAS THEM THROUGH A POINTER** (round 442, battle/main/misc31 func_800BC460, parked). Probed with cc1_probe.sh on the unit's own flags: a `VECTOR` declared inside an `if` block, whose scoped sibling (`SVECTOR vertex`/`SVECTOR sxy`) is dead by then, still gets its OWN slot above every other local (frame 192, vars 160) - the block-scoped object never lands on the dead one. So when retail writes `addiu a2,$sp,0x60` as `ApplyMatrix`'s output and then reads `lhu 0x60/0x64/0x68`, over an SVECTOR written with `sh` at 0x60/0x62/0x64 and a second SVECTOR's `sh` at 0x68/0x6A, the source must have passed `(VECTOR *)&vertex` (and read `(u16)((VECTOR *)&vertex)->vx`) - spelling it that way puts vertex 0x60, sxy 0x68, zeroVec 0x70 on retail's exact offsets. Corollary measured in the same probe: an ADDRESS-TAKEN scalar or `long[1]` local gets its slot at its first `&`-use, i.e. after every aggregate local's, wherever it is declared (retail's `long p`/`flag` at 0x78/0x7C, in the middle of its aggregate list, is still unexplained - do not spend a round on declaration order, it does not move them).
  The 16 dead bytes such a function can carry in mid-frame ARE reachable: see j-an-unused-local-array-is (a dead `u_long t[4]` gets a slot at expand time), which is the untried half of func_800BC460's 24-byte frame gap.

- id: j-an-unused-local-array-is | keywords: frame, phantom, stack, unused, array, prologue, dse, 0x10
  **RETAIL'S FRAME CAN BE LARGER THAN ANY STORE IN IT: AN ARRAY LOCAL GETS ITS STACK SLOT AT EXPAND TIME, SO `u_long t[4]` THAT NOTHING EVER STORES TO STILL EMITS RETAIL'S `addiu sp,sp,-0x10` AND `.frame $sp,16`** (round 411, slus_006.64/psyq/libgpu_b func_80045B00, 70.27 -> 79.80 with this plus the sibling's ternary clamp). Retail clamps two coordinates to `g_GpuScreenWidth-1`/`g_GpuScreenHeight-1`, builds an 0xE4000000 area word, and allocates a 0x10 frame it never touches - not one access through `$sp` in the whole stub. cc1_probe.sh on the unit's own flags shows `.frame $sp,0` for every scalar spelling of the same body and `.frame $sp,16 / vars=16` the moment a `u_long t[4]` local exists, with `subu sp,sp,16` landing in the first `bltz` delay slot exactly as retail's `addiu sp,sp,-0x10` does, and `subu sp,sp,16` before the `jr`. Scalar locals do NOT do this (an all-register body gives vars=0), so do not go looking for the missing frame in the clamp expression: add the dead 16-byte array, and keep the same array size the retail frame implies (a RECT-sized 8-byte local gives 8).
    Round 436 confirms this on a 821-instruction non-SDK body and fixes WHERE the dead slot lands: a large body's frame is `[args 16][vars][saved regs]`, so 8 dead bytes below the saves push EVERY save slot up by 8 and are indistinguishable from a bigger outgoing-arg block (`grep -o '[0-9A-F]*(\$sp)' <stub>.s` shows retail's saves at 0x18-0x30 and nothing at all at 0x10-0x17, and no `sw` to any stack-arg slot, which rules out a 5-arg prototype). field/main/main FieldMain: adding `u_long scratch[2]` (never referenced) took it from 0x30 to retail's 0x38 frame with retail's exact save offsets, 46 differing lines -> 14, 99.46 -> 99.48, 3284 bytes / 821 insns both sides. Declared first, the dead array took retail's 0x10-0x17 exactly, and the declaration position of the USED locals does not matter (`bankIndex` first vs last is byte-identical).
    Round 445 (menu/main/misc4_260 func_801CF8D8, XenoMenu260, 99.86 -> 100.00 at retail's exact 624 bytes / 156 insns) closes a frame whose OTHER locals are all in registers and whose only stack object is a 2-byte flag pair at 0x20: retail's 0x58 frame puts its ten saves at 0x30..0x54 while the body without the dead local is 0x50 with them at 0x28..0x4C, so EVERY prologue and epilogue line differed (44 lines, one store nowhere near the frame). A dead `u8 unusedFrame[8]` declared directly after the flag pair takes 0x22..0x2F and clears all 44 lines; any size 7..14 is byte-identical (the save block only has to reach 0x30). The park's earlier `u8 probePad[8]; probePad[0] = 0;` had already found the frame but kept the store (157 insns, 628 bytes, 99.33) - drop the store, keep the array. Same body, free check: spelling the loop's `base += 0xF0` as `base += sizeof(MenuCardPrimBlock)` is byte-identical and exposed a wrong `// Size: 0xF0` comment (a stray `u8 unkA0[0x50]` padding field made the struct 0x140), so use `sizeof` for a struct stride even when the literal already matches.
  Companion blocker from the same function, measured with the same probe (11 spellings, none reproduces it): retail keeps THREE pseudos in the clamp - `move a3,a0` (the raw parameter), the `(s16)` truncation done IN PLACE on `$a0`, and the clamp result copied back `move a3,v0` - plus a second copy of the width (`move a2,v0`) so the clamp arm re-forms `addiu v0,a2,-1` instead of reusing the compare's. cc1's cse1 merges those two `W-1` into one register for a ternary, an if/else-if with and without an `else xc = x`, `s16` mirror locals, `s16` parameters, a CLIP16 macro with the limit textually duplicated, and `s16`/`u_short` copies of the limit; every one coalesces the result into the parameter's register. Park that residual.

- id: j-a-narrow-stack-argument-is | keywords: stack, parameter, aggregate, struct, lhu, prologue, assign_parms, hoist
  **A NARROW (u16/u8) STACK PARAMETER IS LOADED IN THE PROLOGUE BY cc1 2.6.0 - `assign_parms` emits `lhu`/`lbu` from the incoming slot as the function's FIRST insn - and declaring that parameter as a 4-BYTE AGGREGATE is what moves the load to its first use.** Round 402, battle/misc16 func_8008F0A8: 100.00 at retail's exact 1596 bytes / 399 insns, from a park that had sat at 80.42% for three rounds because retail reads `lhu a0,0x30(sp)` inside the y2 store and the plain `u16 arg4` body reads it at the top of the block. The RTL dump proves where the load comes from: `-dl` on `build/src/<unit>.c.i` shows it as insn 16, BEFORE `NOTE_INSN_FUNCTION_BEG`, so it is not a scheduling choice and no statement order, cast, temp or `do{}while(0)` reaches it. Probe (cc1_probe.sh, gcc-2.6.0-psx, -O2 -G8, 5th parameter, first use in the third statement): `u16`, `s16`, `u8`, and a K&R-declared `u16` all emit the load at the top; a 4-byte `struct { u16 vx; u16 vy; }` keeps the parameter in the caller's frame and emits `lhu $2,16($sp)` at the use. Two more constraints from the same match: the aggregate re-loads per use (an aggregate MEM is invalidated by the volatile stores between uses, 3 `lhu`s in a 3-use probe), so the division has to be captured in a local ONCE - and the capture has to be an EMBEDDED ASSIGNMENT in the store (`y2 = top + (half = (arg4.vx - 16) / 2);`), because a plain `half = ...;` statement one line earlier scores 97.67 with the load one slot early and the `srl` temp in `$v0` where retail has `$v1`. A 2-byte aggregate is passed in a register (o32) and gives no stack load at all, so the type must be exactly 4 bytes. Report the function ROUGH and say the parameter is an aggregate on purpose: the ABI is unchanged (one word in the arg slot) but the caller in the same TU has to pass a 4-byte struct. **It only moves a NARROW load, though: a WORD read of the same 4-byte aggregate still hoists.** Round 403 measured it on the neighbour func_8008E430 (parked 98.39%, retail's exact 1600 bytes): its 5th argument is read with `lw 48(sp)` inside the y0 store, and the aggregate gives 98.20 - byte-identical to the plain `s32 arg4` body, with the `lw` and its sum hoisted above the preceding x0 store just as before, and `s32 *pHeight = &arg4.height` is identical too (the load stays a hoisted `lw`). So reach for the aggregate when the parameter is `u16`/`u8` and retail reads it `lhu`/`lbu` at a use; for a word parameter the load is a scheduling choice, not `assign_parms`. **THE COST IS AT EVERY OTHER CALL SITE IN THE SAME TU, AND IT CAN BLOCK A NEIGHBOUR**: a 4-byte BLKmode aggregate is always handed over with a BLOCK COPY - measured on gcc-2.6.0-psx with cc1_probe.sh from a local, from a cast of the parameter, and from a global (`lwl/lwr/swl/swr`) and from a braced compound literal (`sh` + `sh`) - so no C call passes one with the single `sw $s4,0x10(sp)` a scalar argument costs. Round 412, battle/main/misc16 func_8008F6E4 (the function this lever's aggregate landed on): retail's call to the now-100.00%-matched BattleBuildTileRow3Prims stores ONE word in the jal delay slot, i.e. its source passes a scalar, and the enforced aggregate drops the caller from 69.38 to 65.12 (136 insns against retail's 132, one extra `$s6` save/restore pair on top of the block copy) with no source spelling closing it - swapping that callee's 5th parameter back to `u16` returns it to 80.42, a 19.6-point loss that no gain on the caller repays. So before declaring a matched callee's parameter an aggregate, grep the unit for its callers and check each call site's argument store: one `sw` there means the aggregate cannot be reproduced and the caller becomes unlinkable-in-C (report SIGNATURE CONFLICT, park the caller, keep the callee).

- id: j-two-loop-uses-of-a-stack-local-address-become-a-spilled-invariant | keywords: frame, spill, hoist, loop, address, invariant, move-movables, rect
  **A `&local` USED TWICE IN ONE LOOP BODY IS HOISTED INTO THE PREHEADER BY 2.6.0's loop.c `move_movables` AND THEN SPILLED, WHICH COSTS 8 BYTES OF FRAME AND SHIFTS EVERY CALLEE-SAVED REGISTER; REDUCING IT TO ONE USE IN THE LOOP MAKES reload REMATERIALISE THE `addiu` AND GIVES RETAIL'S FRAME** (round 438, battle/main/misc17 func_80092784, 70.43 -> 86.30%, then measured on the same body). Retail is `jal BattleLoadImageAndSync / addiu a0,sp,0x18` in two places inside the tile loop (the address rematerialised per call, frame 0x90 with 9 saved values s0-s7+fp); the body with both calls taking `&tensRect` emits `addiu t0,sp,0x18` in the preheader, `sw t0,0x68(sp)` beside it, `lw a0,0x68(sp)` at each use, a 0x98 frame, and it then rematerialises the constant 6 as three `li t0,6` because it is one register short. Writing the call ONCE after the if/else (the source in a `pTens` local) removes the hoist: frame 0x90, retail's slots, `addiu a0,sp,0x18` in the delay slot - but the body loses the second call (243 insns against retail's 252). A pointer local for the second use (`pRect = &tensRect; BattleLoadImageAndSync(pRect, ws);`) is copy-propagated away and measures byte-identical, so the two shapes are exclusive in 2.6.0: check whether retail really has BOTH calls before taking the frame win. Same round, a source pointer for a local array of allocated buffers (`*(pSlot = pBufs + i) = pBuf;` - battle/main/misc3's BattleLoadActionRowGraphics idiom) removes a 4*i biv that `pBufs[i]` creates, and writing an `if` as `if (i != 7) {A} else {B}` is what reproduces retail's out-of-line else block and its `beq`.

- id: j-the-bottom-0x10-of-a-mips-frame-is | keywords: frame, 0x10, outgoing, args, scratch, array, size, o32, shift
  **A FUNCTION THAT MAKES ANY CALL HAS A 16-BYTE o32 OUTGOING-ARGS BLOCK AT THE BOTTOM OF ITS FRAME, SO A LARGE SCRATCH ARRAY LOCAL STARTS AT sp+0x10 AND ITS SOURCE SIZE IS RETAIL'S FRAME MINUS 0x10, NOT THE RETAIL OFFSET ITSELF** (round 435, slus_006.64/psyq/libc_sprintf Sprintf, 96.60 -> 96.83 -> 98.46%). Retail puts the format spec at 0x210 and its `p = &work[N]` at sp+0x210, which reads as "`char work[0x210]` at sp+0" and gives a frame 0x10 too big with EVERY sp operand shifted; declaring `work[0x210]` under this compiler gives `.frame $sp,600 # vars=552, regs=8, args=16` against retail's 584, and the tell is that the shifted object (`fmt` at 0x220 instead of 0x210) is the only thing whose offset is visibly +0x10. `work[0x200]` reproduces retail's 0x248 frame, s0-s6 save slots and fmt/ap offsets exactly. Check `grep '\.frame' build/src/<unit>.c.s` - an `args=` field of 16 is that block, and it is present whether or not the source needs stack args.

- id: j-the-callers-single-sw-rules-out-the-aggregate-parameter | keywords: stack, parameter, aggregate, prologue, hoist, lbu, signature, caller, sw
  **BEFORE SPENDING A ROUND ON `j-a-narrow-stack-argument-is`, READ THE MATCHED CALLER'S ARGUMENT STORE: one `sw $sN,0x10($sp)` in the `jal` delay slot proves the callee's 5th parameter is a SCALAR, so retail's late `lbu`/`lhu` of that slot cannot be the 4-byte-aggregate trick and the entry-block order is not reachable that way** (round 446, battle/main/misc16_r2 func_8008FC1C, parked 89.25% at retail's exact 452 bytes / 113 insns, XenoBattle260). The park's whole residual is the entry block: retail's `lbu s2,0x60(sp)` sits after the frame adjust, the `menuColumnPackageCount = 0` store and the a0/a1/a2 copies, keeps its load-delay `nop`, and its `lui/lw g_BattleDisplayState` is hoisted ABOVE `addiu sp,sp,-0x50`, where 2.6.0 emits the incoming-stack-arg load at BB_HEAD (the very first insn, before the frame adjust). A `cc1_probe.sh` of the same body shape reproduces the BB_HEAD form under 2.6.0 AND 2.7.2 AND 2.7.2-cdk, which is what pointed at `assign_parms`; the matched caller BattleInitMenuColumnPrims (asm/battle/matchings/main/misc16_r2) passes the 5th argument with a bare `ori v0,0,0xE` + `sw v0,0x10(sp)` in the jal delay slot, and the lever above measured that a 4-byte BLKmode aggregate is always handed over with a block copy, so the callee cannot be an aggregate without breaking a matched caller. Park on it. Also measured dead on that body: `s32 i = 0;` and a moved `i = 0;` statement, a `u8 count; count = arg4;` local (88.84, 456 bytes), `arg3 += 8` in the for-increment instead of an offset local (89.25, identical), -O3 89.25, -G0 and 2.7.2-cdk do not compile.

symptoms: frame size off, extra stack slot, prologue save order
try: frame stack prologue spill
- id: j-a-spilled-scalar-slot-width-names-its-type | keywords: spill, stack, slot, local, s16, s32, lhu, sra, lw, narrow, type
  **THE WIDTH OF A SPILLED SCALAR'S STACK SLOT NAMES THE LOCAL'S DECLARED TYPE EVEN WHEN THE FIELD IT CAME FROM IS NARROWER: retail reloading a loop-free local with `lw` means the source declared it `s32`, and an `s16` local re-narrows it with `lhu` + `sll`/`sra` at every use** (round 423, field/dialogue/text_box_render func_8007E1C0, a 787-insn park: 61.04% / 3264 bytes with `s16 x, y, w, h` against 68.38% / 3228 with `s32`, retail 3148). The box rect is four `short` fields at `_pad[0x4A]`, but retail stores each into a 4-byte slot (`sw t8,0x28(sp)`) and reloads it with `lw t9,0x40(sp)`; the `s16` body stored `sh` and then paid `lhu` + `sll 16` + `sra 16` per use - about 20 instructions over retail's count on this body. Two companion reads on the same function: the entry narrowing `lhu 0xB2 / sll / sra` (rather than a plain `lh`) says those two fields are `u_short` in the source struct while the two read with `lh` are `short`, and retail's read ORDER is the source's assignment order (x, w, y, h here), so a four-read group's order is worth matching before blaming the allocator for the spill.
- id: j-an-expand-time-slot-is-always-the-lowest | keywords: frame, stack, phantom, aggregate, declaration, slot, spill, dummy
  **AN EXPAND-TIME (ADDRESSABLE) LOCAL ALWAYS TAKES THE LOWEST VAR SLOT WHATEVER ITS DECLARATION POSITION, SO A RETAIL HOLE AT THE TOP OF THE VAR AREA CANNOT BE REPRODUCED WITH A DEAD LOCAL - ONLY A SPILL-ERA PSEUDO CAN SIT ABOVE THE SPILLS** (round 428, field/main/misc func_8008B978, parked 99.92% at retail's EXACT 776 bytes / 194 insns, Default preset). The body needs vars=32 (four 8-byte slots) and retail's hole is the fourth slot (0x28) with its three used spills at 0x10/0x18/0x20; a dead `u8 unused[8]` does grow the frame to retail's 0x58 but takes 0x10, pushing the three spills to 0x18/0x20/0x28 and so swapping all six sp-relative operands (99.84 with no dummy and the frame 8 short, 99.92 with it - 14 differing lines either way). Declaring the dummy FIRST instead of LAST measures identically, so slots are handed out by KIND (expand-time objects first, then alter_reg spills in pseudo order, upward), not by declaration order - extends j-stack-slot-order-is-reverse and j-a-unused-local-array-is. The consequence for a phantom above the spills: it needs a pseudo that alter_reg gave a slot and reload then rematerialised, and no local in a normal body of this shape spills into one. Round 447 confirms it at 8x the size and gives the numbers to stop on: field/dialogue/text_box_render func_8007E1C0 (787 insns) has retail's used locals at its EXACT offsets (rect 0x18, ctx 0x20, x 0x28, y 0x30, w 0x38, h 0x40, all 8-byte rounded) and 56 dead bytes at 0x48..0x7F with no `$sp` reference above 0x43, and a dead `u_long scratch[12]` grows the frame to retail's 0xA8 with retail's save offsets but takes 0x20 and pushes every used local up by 0x30 (68.34% against 68.38 without it, 750 differing rows either way); declared first or last, identical. Sizing it off the frame alone overshoots: `scratch[14]` first gives 0xB0. So on a large body, read the used-local offsets first - if they already match retail, the frame gap is above the spills and no dead local of any size can close it.
- id: j-stack-slot-order-is-reverse | keywords: frame, stack, slot, declaration, order, round, 8-byte, phantom, spill
  **STACK SLOTS ARE 8-BYTE-ROUNDED AND HANDED OUT IN REVERSE DECLARATION ORDER, SO A PHANTOM SLOT'S POSITION IS CHOSEN BY WHERE THE DEAD LOCAL IS DECLARED** (round 416, battle/main/misc17 func_80093B08, parked 92.05% at 1552 bytes against retail's 1588). Retail's whole block of locals is one 8-byte slot per object from 0x20 to 0x87 with a slot at 0x48 that nothing reads; declaring a dead `u_long pad[2]` between `stripRect` and `bufs` DID take a slot and moved `bufs`/`places`/`weaponIds`/`counts` onto retail's exact offsets (0x50/0x58/0x60/0x68) - and still scored 87.54, because that body also had one extra 4-byte int slot above them, so the frame overgrew by 8 and the prologue/epilogue moved. Count the objects retail's frame implies before adding the phantom: 13 slots x 8 = your var area, and one slot too many in either half is worse than the 8-byte shift you are fixing. Also measured in the same function: `s32 ofs = countOfs;` written for the inner loop is copy-propagated away (identical 92.05% and no extra slot), so a second int slot cannot be requested from source this way; the permuter's only find there was a declaration swap (tensRect before nameRect) worth 87.56 -> 92.05.
- id: j-a-constant-assigned-inside-the | keywords: frame, callee-saved, rematerialise, constant, li, s2, live, call
  **A CONSTANT ASSIGNED INSIDE THE GUARD BLOCK THAT USES IT LANDS IN A CALLEE-SAVED REGISTER ACROSS THE CALLS; ASSIGNED AT THE TOP OF THE LOOP BODY IT IS REMATERIALISED AS `li` AT EVERY CALL SITE (3 instructions instead of 1).** Measured round 404, shop_menu/main/misc func_801CDD14: `polyColor = 0x1000;` written at the top of the `for` body, before the `switch` that contains the `SystemRenderStringEntry`/`LoadImage`/`DrawSync` calls, compiled to `li t2,0x1000` at each of the three `func_8002675C` call sites; moving the same assignment to the first statement of the `if (curItemQuantities[...])` block - where retail's `ori $s2,$zero,0x1000` sits - gives retail's single load into `$s2` plus three `sw s2,24(sp)`, and 97.44 -> 97.85 at 1920 -> 1912 bytes with the 200-byte frame unchanged. cc1 rematerialises a constant freely, so a constant's liveness across calls is invisible to it; the guard block is what makes `$s2` (free after the 0x28 rect width dies) the only candidate. Read where retail's `li`/`ori` of the constant is emitted before deciding the assignment's position, and note that a constant placed there can then compete with the next callee-saved constant: unifying the two `0x32 + FONT_LETTER_HEIGHT * i` arguments in the same function made the 0x28 rect width rematerialise instead and dropped the frame 200 -> 192.
- id: j-a-narrow-stack-argument | keywords: stack, frame, parameter, lbu, reload, narrow, spill, u8
  **A 5TH (STACK) PARAMETER RETAIL RE-LOADS WITH `lbu` PER USE CANNOT BE SPELLED: `u32` gives the frame but `lw`, `u8` gives the `lbu` and a copy** (round 400, battle/main/misc21 func_800A2434 and func_800A2704, both parked). Retail reloads the 5th argument from its caller slot at every use (`lbu t0,0x58(sp)` twice in 2434, `lbu t0,0x88(sp)` twice in 2704) and never stores it. Declared `u32 tag` we get the SAME frame (0x48 / 0x78, every other stack slot at retail's offset) but a `lw`; declared `u8 tag` cc1 copies the argument once at entry (`lbu t0,0x60(sp) / sb t0,0x18(sp)` in 2434 -> frame 0x50, 724 bytes against retail's 720; the 2704 twin grew to 0x80 / 1040 bytes / 260 insns against 242) and every later read comes from the copy. So the narrow reload of a QI stack argument is out of reach: keep `u32` (right size, right slots) and park on the `lw`, and do not spend a lane on `u8` variants - the frame moves. The lib_gear twins (func_801DF7F4 70.90%, func_801DFAC4 79.40%) are the same two functions and are stuck on it too.
- id: j-indirect-call-through-a-global | keywords: frame, stack, prologue, jalr, indirect, call, register, slot
  **AN INDIRECT CALL THROUGH A GLOBAL FUNCTION POINTER IS NOT SOURCE-CONTROLLABLE: cc1 puts the target in `$v0` and saves `ra` at 0x10 for every spelling, so retail's `$t1` plus `ra` at 0x14 means a 4-byte frame slot that nothing writes** (round 397, slus_006.64/psyq/libapi_b `_SendPAD`, parked 97.80% at retail's exact 40 bytes / 10 insns, only 2 lines differing). Retail is `lui $t1,%hi(hook) / lw $t1,%lo(hook)($t1) / addiu $sp,-0x18 / sw $ra,0x14($sp) / jalr $t1`; every spelling measured emits `lui $2 / lw $2 / addiu $sp,-0x18 / sw $ra,0x10($sp) / jal $31,$2` with the load already hoisted ABOVE the prologue (so the schedule agrees and only the register and the ra slot are left) and a `.frame` line reading `vars= 0`. `ra` at 0x14 in a 0x18 frame means retail's own `.frame` had `vars= 4` (or a second save at 0x10, and the body has none): one stack slot from a pseudo spilled across the call and rematerialised, i.e. the call insn's symbol operand was live in its RTL - the only shapes that grow the var area from source are address-taken locals, and two of those cost `vars= 8`, never 4. Measured with `cc1_probe.sh` on psx 2.7.2 -O3 -G0: direct `(*hook)()`, a `VoidFn` local assigned from the hook, a cast from `u_long`, `hook[0]` of an array, `hook.field` of a struct, the hook reached through a pointer-to-pointer, a `volatile` hook, an `int`-returning hook, `return (*hook)()`, and `register`-free variants - all identical; `-O1`/`-O2`, gcc-2.6.0 and gcc-2.7.2-cdk are identical too. A 3-minute permuter run (4872 iterations) went base=20 -> best=20. In an SDK unit whose neighbours are hand-patched asm (`sw $ra` to a global, a BIOS vector through `$t2` with `li $t1,0x57`), `$t1` as the jump register is the handwriting style: treat a stub with `jalr $t<0-9>` as asm-origin and park it instead of hunting the allocator. Round 436 closed the frame half of this by arithmetic: `.frame` counts below `ra` are `args + vars`, `args` is fixed at 16 for ANY unprototyped pointer call, and every addressable local rounds to a multiple of 8 (measured on this unit's flags: dead scalars and `u_long t[1]` give `vars= 0`, `u_long t[2]` / `u_short t[2]` / `char t[3]` / a 4-byte struct give `vars= 8`, `u_long t[3]` and `u_short t[6]` give `vars= 16`), so retail's 20 bytes can only be `args= 20` - which needs a 5-word prototype whose argument setup adds instructions - or `vars= 4`, which this cc1 cannot emit. Read a 4-byte frame gap as prologue slack in hand asm, not as a missing local.
- id: j-local-declared-before-another | keywords: stack, frame, extern, callee-saved, cc1, prologue, u8, volatile
  **A local declared BEFORE another one can fix the PROLOGUE**: cc1 saves a callee-saved register immediately before its first definition, so moving `u8 *pScratch` above `Actor *pActor` moved a save by two instructions (round 316, world_map/misc6 func_80080600 80.11 -> 84.32, alongside a scalar extern reached through `extern s16 g_X[]` + `*(volatile s16 *)g_X` to keep its address in a register where `s16 *pLocal = &g_X` folds into two symbol forms).
- id: j-global-s-symbol-address | keywords: stack, frame, addiu, cc1, cse
  **A GLOBAL'S SYMBOL ADDRESS IS A CSE'D CONSTANT, and the leak is a frame slot** (round 335): the same symbol in two statements with a call between them hands both uses ONE pseudo, that pseudo is live across the call, cc1 spills it, reload rematerialises it, so the instruction stream looks byte-identical to retail's and the only trace is a phantom 8-byte local (`vars= 8` where retail has `vars= 0`). Spell the second access through an equivalent base to break the sharing (`D_800X36C0 + 0x60` for `D_800X3720`, the same address): battle/misc3 func_80078658's `addiu $sp` shrank to retail's 24 and it matched. So before blaming an allocator for a frame gap, compare the frame's `vars=` count and look for a symbol used twice across a call.
- id: j-stack-arguments-gcc-puts | keywords: stack, frame
  Stack arguments: gcc 2.6.0 puts arg N (6 and up) at 16 + (N-6)*4 from sp, so a callee reads it at frame_size + 16 + (N-6)*4. When the .s reads a stack arg LOWER than your prototype predicts, the function has FEWER parameters than you declared: battle/misc22 func_800A48EC reads its pOt at 0x54, which is exactly 8 params (round 140).
- id: j-unused-local-right-size | keywords: stack, frame
  AN UNUSED LOCAL OF THE RIGHT SIZE IS HOW YOU GET RETAIL'S FRAME: 'SVECTOR sv;' declared FIRST moved a copy from 0x28 to 0x30, and 'u32 spare2[6];' (24 bytes) declared right after it moved the next group from 0x30 to 0x50, giving retail's exact 0x70 frame with mode@0x30 and &action@0x50 - no instruction touched either slot, so they are dead locals in the original source. 28 bytes or more rounds the slot up to 32 (0x78), so match the measured byte count exactly.
- id: j-prologue-save-pair-order | keywords: stack, frame, loop, move, permuter, prologue, s32, sw
  A prologue save-pair order that will not move: give the parameter its own LIVE COPY (`s32 rows = count;` used in the guard, loop test and size) - gcc 2.7.2 emits the save/copy pair at the copy's position, which reorders the `sw` pairs into retail's order (round 61, world_map/misc9). Same family as the two-statement base split above; try both before a fourth permuter run on a 99%+ body.
- id: j-retail-has-dead-stores | keywords: stack, frame, array, delay, reorg
  When retail has DEAD stores to consecutive stack slots (a `u_long t[4]` that is partly unused and partly read back), a local ARRAY is the lever: gcc 2.x keeps a local array in memory and CSEs the later reads back into registers, so the stores survive with no reload, where four separate scalars give no dead stores and a 0x0 frame. A dead trailing test (`if (g_Sym || y) { return r; } else { return r; }`) is also load-bearing in some bodies: it makes reorg fill a taken branch's delay slot with that arm's first instruction (func_80045BCC went 87.94 -> 98.24 that way).
- id: j-original-has-unused-stack | keywords: stack, frame
  Original has an unused stack slot and yours does not: declare an unused local (e.g. `RECT rect;`) - it reproduces the frame exactly. gcc does NOT drop a declared-but-unused local (round 44: an unused `VECTOR vec;` gave retail's 0x40 frame where a park note had claimed the opposite).
- id: j-byte-phantom-frame-two | keywords: stack, frame, epilogue, prologue
  An 8-byte phantom frame (two register-indexed loads of the same global give a prologue/epilogue that stores nothing) is NOT a wall: matched functions in this repo have it because retail has it too. Do not fight the frame, fight the instruction stream.
- id: j-how-cc1-places-prologue | keywords: stack, frame, prologue, cc1, move, callee-saved, reorg, sw
  HOW cc1 PLACES THE PROLOGUE SAVES, answered by probe (round 304, battle/misc23 func_800A9A50 98.88 -> 100.00): cc1 emits each callee-saved register's sw IMMEDIATELY BEFORE the first instruction that DEFINES that register, and a parameter's move $sN,$aN is that definition. The prologue SAVE ORDER itself (s7,s8,s6,s4,ra,s5,s3,s2,s1,s0) is fixed by the register mask and identical in both compiles. So a "copy in the wrong place mid-prologue" is not a separate lever: it follows where the pseudo's def lands, which statement splitting controls - three splits closed that body: timer = g_A + 1; g_A = timer + g_B; (one expression makes gcc reassociate to (B+1)+A and swaps the loads), step = 0; as its own statement before a clamp if (so reorg steals it into the first bnez slot), and j = 0; as its own statement before pp = g_X; with an empty for-init (so j gets $s2 not $s1 and cse2 can no longer delete a move).
- id: j-stack-pair-stored-reloaded | keywords: stack, frame, lhu, s32, sh, struct, array, extern
  A STACK PAIR STORED AND RELOADED IMMEDIATELY (sh 0x10(sp) then lhu a0,0x10(sp) a few instructions later) IS A STRUCT PASSED BY VALUE, not a memory round-trip. Round 281 matched battle/misc28 func_800B6518 (152 bytes) only after seeing that the already-matched siblings in battle/misc33 declare extern s16 SpriteComputeAngleFromPoints(DVECTOR, DVECTOR) and their asm has the identical sh/lhu/or shape at the same slots - every u16/s16-array spelling of the argument was 5-6% off, and the DVECTOR prototype also decides whether a field is read lh or lhu. So before fighting a read-back, check whether the callee's prototype in another unit takes the value by struct, and check whether the prototypes AGREE across units (misc28 had declared it (s32, s32)).
- id: j-giant-park-instructions-short | keywords: stack, frame, pointer, jal, loop, prologue, struct, u8
  WHEN A GIANT PARK IS 11 INSTRUCTIONS SHORT, CHECK THE ARM ORDER AND THE FRAME BEFORE THE REGISTERS. Round 278 took battle/misc21's 1021-instruction func_8009F844 from 83.78 to 99.25% at retail's EXACT 4084 bytes (1009 vs 1009) with source shapes alone, and the biggest single step (83.78 -> 91.81) was that the tween loop's arms were BACKWARDS: retail is 'if (project3D == 0) { 2D arm inline; continue; } 3D arm ...' with each arm carrying its own allocate:/done: labels inside the branch. A wrong branch polarity is worth 8 points and looks like a register problem from the diff. The second mechanism is the FRAME: a missing 8-byte local was really a second live copy of a pointer (pSprite = pActor->pSprites; pSprites = pSprite;) which sets the whole prologue assignment order and every slot above 0x90. So for a body whose size is exact and instruction count matches, walk the diff SEGMENT BY SEGMENT (jal-delimited) and check each segment's count before touching a register - and prefer re-spelling a walker (a typed struct pointer that gcc can re-base, a u8 count that keeps two live copies) over adding a local.

- id: j-slot-spacing-tells-decl-from-spill | keywords: frame, stack, spill, slot, alignment, himode, vars, probe
  **STACK-SLOT SPACING IDENTIFIES WHICH OBJECTS ARE EXPAND-TIME LOCALS AND WHICH ARE RELOAD SPILLS: DECL SLOTS PACK AT THE TYPE'S NATURAL ALIGNMENT (4 for SImode, 2 for HImode), SPILL SLOTS SIT 8 APART - so a retail frame whose slots alternate 4-apart and 8-apart is a mixture, and the 4-apart ones cannot be spills** (round 415, slus_006.64/system/rendering func_8001D53C, 363 insns, parked 79.36%). Measured with `cc1_probe.sh` on the unit's own flags: eight addressable locals (u8* / u8* / u16 / u16 / u32 / u32 / u8 / u8*) come out at 0x10,0x14,0x18,0x1A,0x1C,0x20,0x24,0x28 (`vars= 32`), i.e. natural packing, while the same locals spilled by pressure come out every 8 bytes. Retail's frame is 0x68 with `.frame`-equivalent saves 0x28 + home 0x10 + vars 0x30 and slots 0x10 pFrames, 0x14 pOffsets (4 apart = DECL slots), 0x18 texY, 0x20 defaultMode (8 apart = spills), 0x28 color, 0x2C count (decl), 0x30 unkFlag (spill), 0x38 pSpriteData (spill), last end 0x3C -> align8 0x40 + 0x28 = 0x68 exactly. The same body written the obvious way spills all eight (i, pFrames, pOffsets, unkFlag, pSpriteData, texY, defaultMode, color) at 0x10,0x18,0x20,...,0x48, last end 0x4C -> 0x78: 16 bytes of frame and every sp-relative operand, from slot KIND rather than from an extra live value - so do not read a frame gap as 'one register too many' before counting how many slots are 4-apart. Note the same body also saves `$fp` without using it (fp appears only in the prologue/epilogue), which is how it holds eight values in s0-s7 where retail holds nine.
- id: j-a-union-aggregate-parameter-buys | keywords: aggregate, struct, union, frame, temp, compound, literal, sw, sh, block, copy, lhu
  **A UNION {u32 word; u16 field;} PARAMETER IS HOW THE AGGREGATE'S CALLER GETS ONE `sw` BACK, BUT IT COSTS THE FRAME: the SImode union makes the caller store retail's single word (132 insns / 528 bytes, retail's own count) only by materialising the value in a stack TEMP, so the frame goes 56 -> 64, and it costs the callee its match (the word load is hoisted and narrowed with `andi` instead of retail's `lhu` at the use: 100.00 -> 99.20)** (round 426, battle/main/misc16 func_8008F6E4, the caller in the entry above). So the align-2 4-byte struct that keeps the callee's `lhu` is the only shape worth shipping, and its `sh`+`sh` argument copy plus the extra callee-saved register the hoisted setup forces (135 insns / 540 bytes) is the price - do not trade a matched callee for the caller. Two findings on the caller worth keeping: retail forms the quad's edges ONCE before the stores, so `right = arg1 + arg3; bottom = arg2 + arg4;` as u16 locals is 65.12 -> 68.42, and spelling the store base index-first `(g_BattlePrimStartIndex + p->prims)->x0` gives retail's `addu v0,v0,s2` instead of `addu v0,s1,v0` (68.42 -> 68.50).
- id: j-a-redundant-parameter-copy-local | keywords: prologue, save, order, parameter, copy, local, pseudo, sparse
  **AN UNNECESSARY POINTER LOCAL INITIALISED FROM A PARAMETER (`s32 *pX = (s32 *)arg3;`) REORDERS THE PROLOGUE'S PARAMETER-COPY PAIRS, SO A `sw s5,84(sp) / move s5,a3` BEFORE `sw s8,96(sp) / move s8,a1` RESIDUAL IS SOURCE-REACHABLE AFTER ALL - through the pseudo shape, not the statement order** (round 423, menu/main/misc4_260 func_801CDC6C, XenoMenu260, 99.17 -> 99.29 with nothing else changed: 2 differing lines instead of 6, and the whole prologue became retail's). The park had blamed a scheduler/flag wall for three rounds (levers.md c-two-loads-hoist-the records four flag sets and the a3/a1 pair "not source-reachable"), but the permuter's find was to drop the cast-holding local and spell the four uses `((s32 *)arg3)[12]` in place: with the extra pseudo gone the copy order fell out, and the same edit did not disturb the frame, the saved set or any other instruction. So when a prologue's save/copy pairs are in the wrong ORDER (not the wrong registers or a shifted frame), list the locals that merely rename a parameter and try removing each one's initialiser before parking on the scheduler; the residue left here is only the two-load `li` placement of c-two-loads-hoist-the.
- id: j-the-frame-and-the-slot-offsets | keywords: frame, stack, phantom, dummy, aggregate, offsets, reload, slot
  **WHEN THE ONLY RESIDUAL IS THE FRAME, CHECK WHERE THE REAL SPILLS LAND FIRST: if they are ALREADY on retail's offsets, no dummy local can close it, because an expand-time object always takes the LOWEST slot (0x10) and pushes every spill up by its size, while the frame and the slot offsets are one quantity** (round 414, field/main/misc func_8008B978, Default preset, parked 99.92% at retail's exact 776 bytes / 194 insns). Retail's 0x58 frame holds three 4-byte spills at 0x10/0x18/0x20 and 8 bytes at 0x28 that no instruction touches; without any dummy this body gives retail's three spills at retail's own 0x10/0x18/0x20 and only `addiu sp,sp,-0x50` plus all ten saves 8 low (99.84), while `u8 unused[8]` declared in the function gives the 0x58 frame with every spill 8 high (99.92). The arithmetic behind that: allocations start at 0x10, the frame is `align8(last_offset_end) + 40`, so the missing bytes need an allocation that ENDS above 0x28 (a 4-byte slot at 0x28 is enough) - i.e. a slot handed out at ALLOCATION time after the spills, the signature of a pseudo `alter_reg` gave a slot and reload then rematerialised (an address constant, presumably). So measure the no-dummy body first: if its spills already sit on retail's offsets and only the frame is short, the park is a phantom reload slot and no declaration closes it; if the spills are shifted, the dummy size/shape is the lever (see j-unused-local-right-size). Presets ruled out on the same body: gcc-2.6.0-psx 97.84, cdk not measured.
- id: j-a-constant-address-pseudo-cannot-be-made-to-spill | keywords: frame, spill, rematerialise, remat, constant, address, symbol_ref, slot, hoist
  **A PSEUDO HOLDING A CONSTANT ADDRESS CANNOT BE FORCED INTO A SPILL SLOT FROM C: reload rematerialises it at each use instead, so a `sw`-and-two-`lw` slot (3 instructions) loses to two `lui`+`addiu` rebuilds (4) and the frame stays one slot short no matter how the pointer is spelled** (round 382, world_map/main/misc16 func_800983A0, 259 insns, XenoWorldMapDiv, parked 88.71% / 1040 bytes against retail's 1036 / .frame 0x70). Retail computes four scratchpad pointers in the ROW loop and spills two (`addiu t6,s2,%lo(D_1F8000C0); sw t6,0x38(sp)`, 0xC8 to 0x40(sp)) while keeping 0xE0 and 0xD0 in `$s6`/`$fp` - eleven live values, nine saved plus two spilled. This body reaches ten: `(Point*)(pScratch + 0xC0)` IS spilled (a PLUS of a register and a constant, one `addiu` to remat), `(Point*)(D_1F8000C8)` is not, because a symbol_ref is cheaper to rebuild than to reload, so the frame is 0x68 and the body one instruction long - the same arithmetic as j-the-frame-and-the-slot-offsets, reached from the other side. Companions measured on the same body: assigning all four inner pointers at the top of the row loop (retail's own shape) makes gcc HOIST them into the prologue as loop-invariant constants and reach 1036 bytes / 259 insns, retail's exact size, but the three constants the hoist keeps alive take `$s2`-`$s4`, the scratchpad base drops `$s2` -> `$s1` and the body falls to 83.24%; writing the fourth corner as `pScratch + 0xB8` (base-derived, which is how retail pools the base and forms it with `ori`) is 1036 / 81.45. So on this class of park, size and register allocation are mutually exclusive and the note should record both numbers rather than the higher one alone.

- id: j-a-second-source-pseudo-of-a-parameter-buys | keywords: spill, parameter, pseudo, reload, frame, register, edge, index
  **A PARAMETER THAT RETAIL RELOADS FROM A STACK SLOT WHILE ALSO KEEPING IT IN A CALLEE-SAVED REGISTER IS TWO SOURCE PSEUDOS OF THE SAME VALUE - ADD THE LOCAL COPY AND USE IT ONLY WHERE RETAIL RELOADS (79.36 -> 80.76%, 1424 -> 1436 bytes on a 363-instruction body)** (round 430, slus_006.64/system/rendering func_8001D53C, XenoRendering, parked). Retail keeps pSpriteData in `$s5` for the walk's pBase->pDirTransforms and the final field_0x40 store, yet the clut arm reads flags_0xA8/field_0x7C through `lw t0,0x38(sp)`. Adding `SpriteData* pOwner = pSpriteData;` (assigned in the preheader, used only in that arm) reproduced the extra slot and 3 instructions and raised the score, and it left the source pseudo set identical to retail's. So before reading a parameter reload as a spill, check whether the register copy is still live across it: if it is, the reload is a second variable in the original source and the local copy is free to add.
  Companion measurement from the same body, on the residual that survived: **gcc 2.7.2's giv combination picks the base of a pointer induction variable by REFERENCE COUNT, never the pointer itself**, so a struct walk with a hot field at +0x14 and a pair at 0x0/+0x2 emits a SECOND induction base (`addu $18,$fp,2` in the preheader, both bases bumped by 24 in the loop) and one callee-saved register is gone; retail's single `$s0 = pFrameData` with displacements 0/2/4..0x14 is not reachable by reordering the stores (moving them onto another base makes gcc pick +0x14 as the base instead - `lw 0($18)`, `sb -11($18)` - and the frame grows), by typing them as a `u16*` local (CSE'd back), or by flipping the arms (77.00%). Freeing the register this way does NOT put the loop counter back: gcc keeps it in memory regardless, so the two symptoms are one source-shape problem, not two.

- id: j-declaration-order-sets-the-reload-slot-order | keywords: frame, spill, declaration-order, slot, offsets, reload, vars, pressure
