  **IN A HIGH-PRESSURE FUNCTION WHOSE LOCALS ARE ALL *RELOAD-SPILLED* (never address-taken), THE DECLARATION ORDER DECIDES THE `$sp` SLOT ORDER AND THEREFORE THE FRAME SIZE: sorting the declarations into retail's own slot order moved a body from 0xC0/vars=0x80 to retail's exact 0xC8/vars=0x88 and 89.83 -> 90.12%** (round 445, field/main/misc8 func_80084158, parked 90.12%, Default preset, 503 insns / 2012 bytes against retail 501 / 2004). Retail's slots run 0x40 groundY, 0x48 pFieldActor, 0x50 refX, 0x58 refZ, 0x60 floorY, 0x68 scriptFlags, 0x70 contact, 0x78 hitCount, 0x80 savedCanInteract, 0x88 + 0x90 the two masks; the body's first version had scriptFlags 0x50, savedCanInteract 0x58, hitCount 0x60, contact 0x68, refX 0x70, refZ 0x78, floorY 0x80 - the same eleven slots in a different order, i.e. the variables were all present and all spilled, only the hand-out order was wrong. Declaring `s32 refX; s32 refZ; s32 myY; s32 floorY; u_int scriptFlags; s32 contact; s32 hitCount; s32 savedCanInteract; s32 nearest; s32 groundY; s32 otherY; ...` reproduced retail's map and the frame exactly (216 differing lines from 260). Note this is gcc 2.7.2's *reload* slot assignment, so it is the opposite of j-an-expand-time-slot-is-always-the-lowest (addressable locals ignore declaration order); `grep -oE '[0-9]+\(\$sp\)'` on your own `build/src/<unit>.c.s` compared against the stub's is the whole measurement. What it did NOT fix: which register each spilled value's load lands in, so the park stayed on the allocator (i `$s3` vs retail `$s2`).
  Also measured on that body, worth knowing before trying to force a CSE across a flag store: with the other actor's `scriptFlags.flags` read inline at its three uses gcc RELOADS it each time (`lw v0,0(s0)` x3) even though only `0x4(s0)` is stored in between, and naming it in a local gives retail's single load - but that load then takes `$s2` (retail has it in `$s4`) and drags `i` down one register, so the two were pull-against-each-other at 90.08 vs 89.83.

- id: a-volatile-stack-read-hack-sets-the-frame-by-its-element-count | keywords: frame, stack, volatile, uninitialised, local, slot, size, array
  **WHEN RETAIL READS AN UNINITIALISED STACK SLOT AND YOUR BODY REPRODUCES IT WITH A VOLATILE ARRAY LOCAL, THE ARRAY'S ELEMENT COUNT IS THE FRAME-SIZE KNOB: trim it until the `addiu sp,-N` and every save offset match** (round 451, lib_gear/main/main func_801E5D44, 97.55 -> 97.61% and frame 0x90 -> retail's 0x88, 1512 bytes / 378 insns, XenoLibraryDiv). Retail reads `lhu t7,0x50(sp)` (its first local slot) into a signed local; `volatile u16 unkFlag[8]` reproduced the read at the right offset but cost 8 bytes of frame (prologue 144 vs retail 136), and `volatile u16 unkFlag[4]` gives retail's frame and a byte-exact prologue/epilogue. Read the retail frame size out of the `.s` save offsets before choosing the count.
- id: j-a-derived-index-local-across-a-call-costs-the-frame | keywords: spill, callee-saved, frame, index, stride, local, recompute, expression
  **AN INDEX THE SOURCE READS OUT OF A STRUCT OUT OF A GLOBAL, MASKED AND SCALED BY A STRIDE (`g_GameState.characters[pManager->currentCharacterIDs[i]].gearId`), MUST BE WRITTEN AS THE FULL EXPRESSION AT EVERY USE - A ONE-LINE LOCAL FOR IT IS KEPT IN A CALLEE-SAVED REGISTER ACROSS THE `LZSSHeapDecompress` CALL, AND THAT ALONE COSTS THE FRAME AND 20 INSTRUCTIONS** (round 451, menu/main/misc1_a func_801C72BC, XenoMenu260 = gcc-2.6.0, 92.91 -> 99.98% and 2048 -> retail's exact 2128 bytes / 532 insns; the only two remaining instructions are the `lui %hi`/`lw %lo` of the unit-owned `jtbl_801C5048`, i.e. a JTBL match, check_bin OK on `menu`). The park-free body had `u8 gearId = MenuGetSlotGearId(i);` and gcc saved s0-s5 + ra (frame 0x28 -> 0x30) where retail saves s0-s3 + ra; writing the accessor as a macro (three uses in the loop body: the `!= GEAR_ID_NONE` test, the pTable argument and the store's index, plus the call argument after it) reproduces retail exactly, because retail recomputes the whole `lbu` / `0xA4`-stride chain TWICE per iteration - once before the call for the test and the argument, once after it for the store index and the callee's argument. Two facts that made the identification quick: the .s `lbu %lo(g_GameState+0x30C)($at)` with the `(i*4+i)*8+i)*4` chain is `g_GameState.characters[i].gearId` (GameCharacter's 0xA0 field at the 0x26C array), and a `u8` loop counter would add `andi 0xff`/`sltiu` where retail has `addiu`/`slti`, so `i` must be `s32`. Same mechanism as j-re-fetch-the-table-not-a-cached-pointer, reached from a derived scalar rather than a pointer, and it is the same rule as a-scaled-index-local-buys-the-last-callee-saved-register with the sign flipped: there the offset local was the fix, here the index local is the bug - the tell is whether retail recomputes the chain on both sides of the call.

## struct/typed spelling and relocations

symptoms: lui/addiu vs gp, address-of-global shape, cast vs field
try: struct lui gp address cast
- id: i-rodata-owner-blob-frees-a-whole-unit-to-conversion | keywords: rodata, dlabel, blob, convertible, blocked, layout, yaml, ownership
  **A UNIT WHOSE RODATA RANGE IS OWNED BY ITS STUBS IS NOT BLOCKED: changing its splat config entry from `[<off>, .rodata, <unit>]` to `[<off>, rodata]` links the same bytes in the same order as an anonymous blob object, so NO converted function has to carry the data and every stub in the range becomes convertible, one at a time, in any order** (round 432, field/main/misc8, `make check` all eleven OK with no C changes). Before the change `linker/field.ld` placed only `build/src/field/main/misc8.c.o(.rodata)` at the range start and splat put the `dlabel`s in the stub `.s` files in address order, so `cc1` emits C constants `.rdata` BEFORE `.text` while the stub's `.section .rodata` appends at its `.include` point: converting the MIDDLE owner (func_80084158, 0x158..0x170) moved its two strings to the range start and pushed the earlier owner's five up, i.e. the class AGENTS.md documents for misc2's `D_8006FB70`, and `convertible.py` had all three owners BLOCKED. With `[0x11C, rodata]` the ld places `build/asm/field/data/11C.rodata.s.o(.rodata)` there instead, splat emits the blob with all nine dlabels in retail order, no stub `.s` owns data any more, and `convertible.py` lists 17 of 17 convertible. Two limits: a converted function must reference those strings as `extern const char D_XXXXXXXX[]` and emit NO rodata of its own (a unit with this config gets its `c.o(.rodata)` placed in the ld's no-range tail group, i.e. the wrong address, so a new string literal in that unit silently shifts the overlay), and the change is one line per unit in `config/<bin>.yaml` proved by `make check` before any C is touched.
- id: k-a-member-lvalue-keeps-the-offset-out-of-the-lo16 | keywords: lui, lo16, displacement, fold, struct, member, address-of, cast, member-lvalue
  **AN OFFSET WRITTEN INTO THE ADDRESS (`sym + i * STRIDE + OFF`) IS FOLDED INTO THE SYMBOL'S `%lo` (`addiu base,base,OFF` + `lhu 0`), AND SO IS `&((View *)sym)->member`; ONLY THE MEMBER-LVALUE FORM `((View *)(sym + i * STRIDE))->member` (dot on a cast POINTER, or `.field` on a struct value) LEAVES IT IN THE LOAD DISPLACEMENT, which is retail's `addiu base,base,0` / `lhu 0x172(base + i * 0x48)`** (round 453, battle/main/misc30 func_800B9C78, cdk -G0: 98.93 -> 99.51% at retail's exact 768 bytes / 192 insns, the row reads in six switch arms). Probed with `tools/scripts/cc1_probe.sh battle/main/misc30` on four spellings of the same address: `*(u16 *)(g_W + g_I * 0x48 + 0x14E)` and `*(u16 *)((u8 *)(g_W + g_I * 0x48) + 0x14E)` and `((u16 *)(g_W + g_I * 0x48))[0xA7]` all give `lui $3,%hi(g_W+334)` + `lhu 0($2)`; `((Row *)(g_W + g_I * 0x48))->unk14E` gives `lui $3,%hi(g_W)` / `addiu` / `lhu 334($2)`; and `(&((Base *)g_W)->row)->unk0` (an address-of, even of a struct member) folds again, while `((Base *)(g_W + g_I * 0x48))->row.param` does not. So the shape the member offset must come from is an LVALUE, and a two-level `->row.field` is fine as long as there is no `&`. The reason the "correct" typed spelling in that unit (`BATTLE_COMMAND_ROW(i)` = `&((BattleWorkRows *)g_BattleStateWork)->rows[i]`) folds is that its 0x14E is a constant SIBLING of the symbol; cycling it through a byte-offset pointer first (`(Base *)(g_BattleStateWork + i * 0x48)`) is what pushes the constant to the MEM. Practical shape: a pad-then-row struct whose first member is the row, reached as a member lvalue of the cast pointer.
- id: k-a-pointer-local-minus-a-constant-still-folds-into-the-lo16 | keywords: lui, lo16, negative, base, symbol, fold, pointer, volatile
  **`(u8 *)&g_ScalarGlobal - K` IS FOLDED INTO THE `%lo` EVEN THROUGH A POINTER LOCAL, so retail's "one `la` of the symbol, then `addiu reg,reg,-K` off the same register, then a load through that register" is not reachable from C when the two addresses are the same symbol at a negative offset** (round 443, field/main/misc11 FieldProjectActorOriginToScreen, parked 95.06% at retail's EXACT 192 bytes / 48 insns; the same wall is misc8's FieldActorWorldToScreenPosition at 95.25). Retail's first `CompMatrix` argument is `&g_FieldActors - 0xAC` (= g_Scene.worldToScreenMatrix, 0x800AFA64 vs the pointer global at 0x800AFB10): `lui a1,%hi(g_FieldActors) / addiu a1,a1,%lo(g_FieldActors) / addiu a0,a1,-0xAC / ... / lw a1,0(a1)`. Writing `FieldActor* volatile* ppActorList = &g_FieldActors; CompMatrix((MATRIX *)((u8 *)ppActorList - 0xAC), &(*ppActorList)[idx].childMatrix, ...)` gives the exact same instruction COUNT but `lui a0,%hi(g_FieldActors) / addiu a0,a0,-172` for the matrix plus a separate `lui a1,%hi / lw a1,0(a1)` for the pointer load - the -0xAC is folded into a second symbol address, which is the negative form of k-a-negative-index-offset-needs-a-pointer-local-to-stay-a-displacement. Naming `&g_Scene.worldToScreenMatrix` instead emits two independent %hi/%lo pairs (one more instruction of setup). Because the matrix sits at a NEGATIVE offset from the symbol retail anchors on, a parent struct cannot express it: a parent symbol at 0x800AFA64 produces the MIRRORED base-first shape (matrix at +0, `addiu reg,reg,0xAC` for the pointer), a different instruction stream. Park it for a symbol-table + shared-header decision.
- id: k-a-disassembled-base-offset-is-a-reloc | keywords: reloc, lo16, %lo, symbol, scratchpad, fp, base, objdiff
  **A `%lo(sym)` IN THE EXPECTED `.s` CAN BE A DISASSEMBLER ARTEFACT RATHER THAN RETAIL'S SPELLING: when a base register holds the matching `%hi`, spimdisasm names the displacement, and the expected object then carries a relocation that no C spelling of base+offset reproduces - so that function cannot read objdiff 100.00 even with a perfect instruction stream** (round 433, world_map/main/misc14 func_80095414, parked 60.05 -> 64.84%). Retail forms the scratchpad probe address as `addiu $a0,$fp,%lo(g_WorldMapStepProbePos)` in five places after `lui $fp,0x1F80`; assembling that `.s` puts `R_MIPS_LO16 g_WorldMapStepProbePos` (value 0x1F800060, so the low half is 0x60) on the instruction. `pStep->probe` off a live base pointer gives `addiu $4,$24,0x60` with NO reloc, and `&g_WorldMapStepProbePos` gives the two-instruction `lui/addiu` la with no base register - one instruction longer and the wrong shape. The linked bytes are identical either way (which is why `check_bin.sh` says OK), so this is the mirror of the func_80095CD4 note: there retail had the literal and our symbol spelling added the reloc; here retail's `.s` has the reloc and our base+offset has none. Before hunting 100.00 on a world_map/scratchpad park, grep the stub's `.s` for `%lo(g_` and check whether the base register is a `lui` of the same page: if it is, budget the function at ~99.9% and spend the lane on the instruction stream.
- id: k-a-constant-address-fold-cannot-be-split | keywords: symbol, addend, fold, la, addiu, constant, address, struct
  **GCC 2.6.0 FOLDS EVERY CONSTANT ADDED TO A STATIC SYMBOL ADDRESS INTO ONE ADDEND, SO A STORE SHAPE `la base / addiu t,base,CONST / addu t,idx / sb 0(t)` IS NOT SPELLABLE AND NO SPELLING SEARCH IS WORTH A LANE THERE** (round 429, menu/main/misc9 func_801DB920, parked 97.10% / 1072 bytes, 4 OVER retail's 1068 / 267 insns; the unit is XenoMenu260). Retail clears `g_GameState.itemIDs[idx]` (addend 0x1F90 + 0x96) with base, then an immediate 0x96, then idx; 19 spellings were measured and every one either folds to ONE symbol addend (`sb $0,g_GameState+8230(idx)`, 1 insn, in context 1064 = one short of retail: `itemIDs[idx]`, `itemQuantities[150+idx]`, `itemQuantities[idx+150]`, `(u32)` cast, `base += 150` then `[idx]`, `&itemQuantities[150]` then `[idx]`, `(u8*)` pointer+const, a 2-D `((u8(*)[150])base)[1][idx]`) or keeps the 150 only when it is a RUNTIME value (a `s16` local materialises it as `li`, 2 insns = one over; a `u8*` local assigned at the site is hoisted and CSEd with the existing base+idx pointer, giving `sb $0,150(reg)` and losing 5). A standalone cc1 probe reproduces the fold with the same unit flags, and only a POINTER PARAMETER (value unknown to the compiler) keeps `addiu $2,$17,$18` - i.e. the split needs a runtime base, which a static symbol never is. Presets screened and dead: 2.7.2 93.64, 2.7.2-cdk 90.70, 2.6.0 -G0 97.10, -O3 97.10.
- id: k-a-scaled-index-not-a-base-pointer | keywords: cse, base, pointer, cast, scaled-index, at, displacement, struct-array
  **WHEN RETAIL RE-FORMS `lui $at,%hi(sym+K) / addu $at,$at,<scaled index> / ld-st %lo(sym+K)($at)` FOR EVERY ELEMENT ACCESS, THE ORIGINAL KEPT ONLY THE SCALED INDEX IN A REGISTER AND THE C MUST NOT MATERIALISE `&array[i]` AS A VALUE - every cast used to reach an opaque sub-object does, and gcc then CSEs `sym + scaled` into one callee-saved register and turns all the accesses into base+displacement** (round 431, field/dialogue/text_box_render func_8007F8DC, parked 73.38% / 1684 bytes against retail's 1804; Default preset, -O2 -G8 confirmed best: gcc-2.6.0 71.13, -O1 55.25, cdk does not compile). Retail's string at box+0x18 is reached eight times in a 0x498-byte element array, and each access is that 3-instruction `$at` form while the `i * 0x498` chain itself is recomputed (seven shifts) in SIX places in fresh registers ($s0/$a0/$a1/$v0) - so the original's cse shared neither the base nor the multiply. Spelling the same eight stores through a typed view struct (`((FieldTextBoxStringView*)g_FieldTextBoxes)[i].string.args[n]`, 0x18 + 0x70 addends folded) measures 73.29% / 1688 bytes and through the header's `_pad` cast 73.38% / 1684: both give gcc ONE base register (`sw $2,136($16)`) and 1-insn stores, i.e. better code than retail, which is why the diff stays at ~73% and 120 bytes short. Same mechanism drives this unit's other box parks (FieldTextBoxDraw 68.38, FieldTextBoxInitializePrimitives, FieldTextBoxInitialize 82.32), so treat "retail recomputes `index * stride` at each access" as a TU-level trait of the original build and park rather than re-spelling. Related and opposite: k-one-base-pointer-for-the-whole-scratchpad, where the base pointer IS retail's shape (a runtime literal base, not a symbol array). Re-measured on FieldTextBoxInitializePrimitives in round 432: dropping the per-sub-struct pointer locals and writing all 40-odd accesses longhand as g_FieldTextBoxes[index].x took 56.13 -> 63.05% (1784 vs retail 1952 bytes), and the residual is exactly this lever's shape plus gcc's choice of symbolic anchor - retail anchors at sym+0xAC and adds `index * 0x498` in a register, every spelling here anchors at the offset of the first field touched (0xC4) and keeps the whole `&g_FieldTextBoxes[index]` in a callee-saved register, so fewer addresses are recomputed. Adding pRect/pHeight walking pointers to the borders loop measured 61.89, i.e. worse, so the loop is not the place to reintroduce locals either.
  Round 444 closed the "which spelling then" question with a probe: **the only C that emits retail's per-access `symbol+const` MEM (gas then expands it to the 3-instruction `$at` form) is a TYPED MEMBER of the declared extern struct** - `g_Box[i].string.args[0]` gives cc1 `sw $2,g_Boxes+136($16)` for EVERY access, so the scaled index is the register part and each member keeps its own symbolic constant. Every cast spelling (`&box._pad[0x38]`, a cast view struct, the `_pad` macro) starts with an address-of and gcc then materialises `sym+scaled` once with `la` and reaches the later members by `addu`. `tools/scripts/cc1_probe.sh` decides this in ~10 s per spelling (probe file with a union member vs an element address). And cc1 2.7.2 rejects an ANONYMOUS union ("structure has no member named"), so a header cannot keep an opaque `_pad` beside a typed member in the same slot - the split is a whole-unit decision, not a worker one. Payoff measured on the same body with `short _pad[0x56]` temporarily re-split: 79.67% / 1724 bytes / 431 insns against retail's 1804 / 451, i.e. the typed member closes 10 of the 30 missing instructions and the rest is the frame and the register set (retail's frame is 0x70: anchor at 0x20/0x24, x at 0x28, top at 0x30, dialogIndex at 0x38, and 0x2C/0x34/0x3C are empty slots of locals that were optimised away). Note also that retail's size is 451 insns = 1804 bytes, so a body 120 bytes SHORT is the healthy direction here: the target is retail's instruction COUNT and address forms, never its byte count.
- id: k-one-base-pointer-for-the-whole-scratchpad | keywords: scratchpad, base, displacement, lui, ori, cse, address, volatile
  **A FUNCTION THAT TOUCHES MANY `0x1F800xxx` SCRATCHPAD ADDRESSES WANTS ONE `u8* pScratch = (u8*)0x1F800000` LOCAL AND `pScratch + 0xNNN` AT EVERY SITE, NOT THE ADDRESS LITERALS: with literals gcc CSEs a separate full address per site (`lui` + `ori`), keeps the two hottest ones in SAVED registers (`0x1F800328`/`0x1F80032C` in `$s6`/`$s8`) and the body grew 37 instructions; with the pointer the base lands in `$s2` and every access is a 16-bit displacement, which is exactly retail's shape** (round 427, world_map/main/misc16 func_8009932C: 64.71% / 284 insns with literals, 92.13% / 250 insns with `pScratch`, 95.05% / retail's exact 247 insns after the second fix below). The second fix, same function: **the store targets of a scratchpad quad must be PLAIN stores - written `*(volatile u16*)(pScratch + K) = *(volatile u16*)(pScratch + J) = expr;` gcc reads `J` back after storing it (a volatile store plus a volatile load of the same address cannot be merged) and pays 2 loads plus 2 nops**; `x1 = expr; *(u16*)(pScratch + J) = x1; *(u16*)(pScratch + K) = x1;` is the 247-instruction form. Loads stay volatile - that is what makes retail reload a value straight after storing it (`sw v0,0x320(s2)` then `lw v0,0x320(s2)`).
- id: k-a-maspsx-big-displacement-splits | keywords: maspsx, displacement, offset, at, lui, lbu, splat, reading, target
  **A DISPLACEMENT OVER 0x7FFF APPEARS IN THE ORIGINAL `.s` AS `lui $at,1; addu $at,base,$at; lbu rt,-0x7AC3($at)`, AND THE OFFSET IS THE SIGN-EXTENDED IMM, NOT THE PRINTED ONE: 0x10000 - 0x7AC3 = 0x853D** (round 420, battle/main/misc2 func_80073B64). The park of that function had copied the printed `-0x7AD3` operands as 0x852D.. (0x10 low) because they look like small negative offsets, which is a semantic error in the switch byte AND in the three load offsets; the tell is the `lui $at,1` / `addu $at,base,$at` pair in front of every load. Read such a load as `base + (0x10000 + imm)` with `imm` sign-extended, and prefer the raw word in the splat comment (`3D852490` -> imm 0x853D) when a note and the bytes disagree.
- id: k-cc1-never-merges-adjacent | keywords: packed, store, constant, u16, u32, merge, sh, sw, pair, probe, prim
  **cc1 NEVER MERGES ADJACENT CONSTANT FIELD STORES, so retail's `sh 0x6010` for (r1,g1) or `sw 0x64707070` for (r0,g0,b0,code) can only come from a WIDE WRITE IN THE SOURCE - write the packed value through a cast on the field pair and the byte count comes out exactly retail's** (round 412, battling/main/misc8 func_80081ECC, 70.16% park -> MATCH at 171 insns / 684 bytes, the old note's claim that "no source type can ask for these stores" was wrong). Probed with cc1_probe.sh on this unit's own cc1: two adjacent `u8` field stores of constants emit `sb/sb`, two adjacent `s16` field stores emit `sh/sh`, and APPENDING them (`setRGB0` + two `sh`) changes nothing - so a prim block whose colours and coordinates are one halfword / one word apart in retail is `*(u16 *)&p->r1 = 0x6010;` / `*(u32 *)&p->x1 = 0x140;` in the source, the same idiom the repo already ships in misc9's BattleSetStageFogColors for the very same D_80095580 quads. Two companions learned here: a read-back of a byte that a wide store wrote cannot be folded (retail's `lbu` + `andi 0xFE` + `ori 0x02` is `setShadeTex(p, 0); setSemiTrans(p, 1);` - `setSemiTrans` alone emits `ori` only, one instruction short), and when several copies of a block take the same constant retail's stores run HIGH INDEX FIRST, i.e. `copies[1].u0 = copies[2].u0 = 0x80;` - the chained assignment emits the right-hand target first and shares one `li` per pair. Store ORDER is otherwise the source's order (with `li` constants hoisted), so keep retail's emitted order when a grouping looks odd.
- id: k-the-address-register-of | keywords: la, lui, addiu, store, register, v0, t0, volatile, probe
  **THE ADDRESS REGISTER OF A ONE-STORE FUNCTION IS NOT A SOURCE CHOICE: every spelling of `*pGlobal = value` emits `la $2` in gcc 2.7.2 and 2.6.0** (round 404, slus_006.64/system/archive_b func_800379B4, parked 96.00% at retail's exact 20 bytes / 5 insns, the only difference being retail's `lui/addiu/sw` through `$t0` against our `$v0`; permuter 5643 iterations base=20 best=20). Probed with cc1_probe.sh in one snippet: `volatile u32 A[3]` dereferenced as `*A`, `A[i]`, a struct field `A.x`, a `(u32 *)u8Array` cast, `*((u32 *)A + 0)`, `&A[0]` and a `u32 *p = A;` local all give `la $2`; dropping `volatile` gives the bare `sw $4,sym` macro form instead (assembler expands through `$at`, 77.80%, 16 bytes). Seven live values would have to precede the pseudo to reach `$8`, and the body has one. cdk -G0 is `lui $2` + `sw %lo($2)` (77.80) and 2.8.1 the same. So a lone-store stub whose only residual is the address register was hand-written or built by an assembler that picked the temp itself: park it after one probe batch, do not permute.
- id: k-retail-holds-member-subobject | keywords: struct, address, pointer, addiu, s32, sw
  **When retail holds a MEMBER-SUBOBJECT address in a register and reaches the struct base by a negative displacement, take the subobject's address into a pointer local and pass the struct separately.** e.g. a s32 pointer to mat.t, three stores through it, then passing &mat to the callee, gives the original's sw 0(a3) + addiu a1,a3,-0x14; the tidy mat.t[0] = ... form is 89.13% (movie/misc2). This is the reverse of the usual typed-pointer advice.
- id: k-a-union-member-with-u16 | keywords: struct, union, alignment, padding, packed, offset, u16, lbu, lhu
  **A UNION MEMBER THAT CONTAINS A `u16` IS ALIGNED TO 2, SO A PAYLOAD SPLIT AS `u8 a; u8 b; u8 c; u16 x` INSIDE ONE INNER STRUCT PUTS `x` AT 0xC WHERE RETAIL READS 0xA - gcc pads after the third byte and the whole access sequence shifts by 2** (round 414, lib_gear/main/main func_801E5D44, 86.68 -> 89.73 in one edit, and the offsets are what the diff had been blaming on the allocator). Check the emitted displacements, not the header's offset comments: retail read `lbu 0x7/0x8/0x9` and `lhu 0xA/0xC/0xE/0x10` from one command struct, ours read `0x8/0x9/0xA` and `0xC/0xE/0x10/0x12`, i.e. everything +2. The fix is to put only the genuinely overlapping bytes in the union (`u8 unk7; union { struct { u8 unk8; u8 unk9; } joint; u16 x; } payload;` then the u16 fields flat after it), which is also the honest reading of a byte-oriented script payload. A comment that says `/* 0xA */ u16 unkA` inside an inner struct is not evidence; compile it and read the displacement.
- id: k-typed-view-also-fixes | keywords: struct, address, lw, sw, cse, jal, s32, sched2
  **A typed view also fixes the LOAD ORDER, not just the address order.** round 315 `battling/misc2`: reading two actors' positions through a typed `BattleQuad` view emits retail's `lw,lw,sw,lw,lw,subu,jal`, where `*(s32 *)(p + 0)` emits `sw,lw,lw,...` - same statements, different order, because the typed operand is what cse and sched2 see. If a park's residual is two loads either side of a store, try the typed view before reordering statements.
- id: k-gcc-26-folds-symbol-plus | keywords: address, symbol, addend, fold, la, addiu, addu, sb, store, probe, offset
  **gcc 2.6.0/-O2 folds a symbol and its constant offset into ONE addend at every spelling, so retail's `la(sym+0x1F90)` + `addiu +150` two-step address is only produced when a REGISTER already holds the base** (round 406, menu/main/misc9 func_801DB920, parked 97.10/1072 = 4 over retail): twelve spellings probed (direct field index, `itemQuantities[150+idx]`, `&arr[150]` then `[idx]`, u8/u32/s16 locals, a two-statement pointer walk, `(u8 *)&struct + 0x1F90 + 150 + idx`) all emit the bare `sb $0,sym+8230(reg)` macro form, and the ones that keep the 150 separate do it by keeping the base live in a callee-saved register (an extra save and 0xC0 of frame). A `short` offset local (found by the permuter, base 874 -> 784) does recover the extra instruction but lands the add on the index side (`li t0,0x96; addu v0,t0,fp; la; addu`), i.e. 6 insns against retail's 5. So a residual that is exactly `la(sym+A)` + `addiu +B` where A+B is the target's real offset is a fold you cannot express from C: park it after one probe batch. **Round 442 addendum: the SIZE half of that residual IS reachable, the operand side is not.** The same body with the offset local declared `unsigned int idOffset` instead of `s16` measures 97.47% at retail's exact 1068 bytes / 267 insns (from 97.10 / 1072 / 268): an unsigned offset keeps the 150 as an `addiu` immediate where the `short` forces a `li`, so the instruction count closes - but cc1 puts that addiu on the INDEX (`addiu v0,fp,150` then `la` then `addu v0,v0,t0`) where retail puts it on the BASE (`la`, `addiu v0,t0,150`, `addu v0,fp,v0`). Index-first `idx + (arr + off)`, a `(s32)` cast on the base and an embedded-assignment pointer local were measured too (the last one also breaks the surrounding preheader, 90.83 / 1056). So: try the offset local's TYPE first, judge a park on the byte count, and only then treat a surviving `addu`/`addiu` operand side as the legitimiser.
- id: k-unbounded-array-extern-global | keywords: struct, address, extern, addiu, array, lui, pointer
  **An unbounded ARRAY extern of the global's own type defeats the address-of-global fold** (round 320): `extern FieldScriptGlobalU16 D_800B2348[];` then `D_800B2348[0].value++;` materialises `lui/addiu` into one register and reuses it, where a scalar, a pointer local, and `(&g)->f` all fold (79.83 -> 100.00). If a park says the fold is unreachable, this is the spelling to try - it is the same family as the `extern s16 g_X[]` + `g_X[0]` lever. Two things it costs, measured in round 396 (world_map/main/misc13 func_80092DF8, 91.17 -> 98.05 at retail's exact 480 bytes/120 insns, still parked): the address-in-a-register form is one instruction shorter AND is the only one whose single `sh` fits a jal delay slot where the symbolic pair needs a `nop` - and the array type is per-DECLARATION, so it takes every other function in the unit that uses the global with it (a matched neighbour fell 100.00 -> 86.14), which means un-parking needs an alias symbol at the same address for the one function, and objdiff then flags the reloc name. A `volatile` deref through a pointer local does not survive the fold either.
- id: k-s32-x-int-x | keywords: struct, address, s32, typedef, signed
  **`s32 x` and `int x` are the SAME TYPE in this repo** (`include/types.h` has `typedef signed int s32`) - round 364 measured a body where swapping them changed nothing. Round 357's report of a 93.97 -> 100.00 jump on that spelling was really the incidental DECLARATION REORDER that came with it, so reorder the declarations, do not reach for the typedef.
- id: k-read-modify-write-needs | keywords: struct, address, array, extern, volatile, addiu, cse, lhu
  **A read-modify-write needs ARRAY DECAY to reach a register** (round 340, ten-spelling `cc1_probe` sweep): retail's `lui/addiu` + `lhu 0($v1)` + `sh 0($v1)` appears when the STORE's address comes from `A[i] = A[i] - 1` on an unbounded array extern, while the signed test loads of the same variable want the plain scalar form - and the array must be the NEIGHBOURING symbol (`D_800B2340[1]` for `D_800B2342`) so cse cannot share its base with the test. A `volatile` on that array extern KILLS the decay (back to the maspsx `$at` macro form), so volatile is always a per-site lvalue, never a blanket qualifier.
- id: k-arm-s-statement-count | keywords: struct, address, la, addiu, cse, case, goto, switch
  **THE ARM'S STATEMENT COUNT DEFEATS A FOLDED `la`, NOT THE ARM'S SHAPE** (round 339): a second `la g_GameState` was being folded into `addiu t2,t2,-900` because cse knows `t2` across the branch. Giving BOTH arms of the test TWO assignments (in menu/misc9: `pIds` and `pQuantities`) makes the arm block no longer single-insn, so cse cannot follow it, and cross-jumping then merges the two identical `addiu`s back into the one retail has after the join - both `la`s survive and the tail is unchanged (96.76 -> 100.00). Every if/else, ternary, one-case switch, goto, `&arr[0]`, do-block and `-O1/-Os/2.7.2/cdk/-G0` spelling folded instead. So when retail materialises one symbol twice and yours folds the second into a displacement, make each arm do one more thing.
- id: k-typed-struct-pointer-always | keywords: struct, address, pointer, u16, callee-saved, volatile
  **A typed struct pointer is not always the realistic form the compiler accepts.** slus_006.64/system/system.c's SystemInitializeFont reads a font-file header as six `*(u16*)(pFontBytes + offsetof(SystemFontFileHeader, field))` and matches at retail's 156 bytes; reading them through a `SystemFontFileHeader *pHeader` local is 61.05% at 164 bytes, because gcc lifts all six loads above the two pointer stores and needs a callee-saved register (same with `volatile` and with a per-field `u16 value;`). Keep the cursor form with the measurement in a comment instead of "cleaning" it, and do the same check before rewriting any header read that shares one pointer.
- id: k-per-slot-battle-register | keywords: struct, address, pointer, addiu, andi, lhu, lui, sll
  **The per-slot battle register file is already typed in three units - read them before spelling `D_800D3420`.** `D_800D3400` is `g_BattleSlotRegs`, 0x40-byte rows: `dwordRegs` at +0x10 (`g_BattleSlotRows`), `wordRegs` at +0x20 (`D_800D3420`), `byteRegs` at +0x30 (`D_800D3430`); battle/misc8.c declares `BattleSlotRow`, battle/misc10.c `BattleSlotRegs`, battle/misc6_r1.c `BattleSlotWordRegs`. Two measured constraints: `g_BattleSlotRegs[i].wordRegs[...]` folds the +0x20 into the load displacement (76.47%), and a pointer local to `wordRegs` alone is 99.41%; only the bank view plus the pointer local reproduces retail's `andi`/`sll` before `lui`/`addiu` and `lhu 0(a2)`.
- id: k-whole-word-script-flag | keywords: struct, address, union
  Whole-word script-flag read-modify-write: use the union scalar member (actor->scriptFlags.flags), never the bitfields.
- id: k-unbounded-array-declaration-materialises | keywords: struct, address, array, volatile, extern, macro, sw
  AN UNBOUNDED ARRAY DECLARATION MATERIALISES THE ADDRESS: 'extern volatile u32 D_X[N];' (array decay, optionally volatile) makes gcc compute the base into a general register for a read-modify-write instead of the 'sw $4,sym' / '%hi'+'$at' macro form. It only does that for the FIRST RMW of a basic block, which is often exactly retail's shape (round 97, world_map/misc5 func_8007ADD4 81.35 -> 87.56).
- id: k-partial-view-struct-cheapest | keywords: struct, address
  A PARTIAL **VIEW** STRUCT is the cheapest way to type a shared actor: declare it in the .c that needs it with only the fields that unit touches and 'unk' for the rest - no header edit, no risk to other units (battling/misc1 went to zero raw casts that way). When TWO units need the same view, give ONE worker both units plus the header (round 90 nested BattleSlotState into BattleTableEntry, round 100 moved the full BattleSprite 0x7C view into actor.h) and make it revert on any regression.
- id: k-prim-offset-local-round | keywords: struct, address, s32, switch
  THE PRIM-OFFSET LOCAL (round 155, menu/misc5 func_801D09F0, 94.75 -> 100): an AddPrim/offscreen-prim base offset that retail loads ONCE into a call-clobbered register wants a source local assigned just before the calls ('s32 primOffset = 0x70;'), not a literal in each argument; the literal folds back per block. Same family as the pre-switch constant idiom above.
- id: k-g-symbol-index-cse | keywords: struct, address, loop, cse, pointer
  THE '&g_Symbol[index]' CSE WALL IS BEATABLE: a loop that reuses the base pointer ('pWork = &D_80077124[0]' inside the loop) makes gcc CSE the address with the preheader value and then clobber the register that held it; give the loop head its OWN variable ('pNext = pWork;') and retail's per-iteration re-forming comes back (round 231, movie/misc1 func_800704E8: 6 differing instructions -> 3, which is the shape AGENTS.md calls unsolved).
- id: k-pointer-local-global-arms | keywords: struct, address, addiu, lbu, lui, pointer, switch, u8
  A POINTER LOCAL FOR A GLOBAL THE ARMS SHARE IS LOAD-BEARING: 'u8 *pArgs = g_BattleCommandArgs;' hoists the buffer address into a1 before the switch (the lui/addiu pair lands between the lbu and the addiu v1,v0,-1); inlining the global into each arm that uses it costs 40 bytes (same function, 96.42%).
- id: k-translate-wide-vectors-long | keywords: struct, address, cast, lw, sh, sw
  Translate wide vectors as long tmp[3] cast to (VECTOR*) to get 32-bit sw/lw; a VECTOR local gives sh.
- id: k-gp-rel-sym-gp | keywords: struct, address, gp, extern
  `%gp_rel(sym)($gp)` vs `%hi/%lo`: maspsx only emits gp-relative when the unit itself defines the symbol in .sdata/.sbss. A plain `extern` gives %hi/%lo.
- id: k-gcc-merges-two-address | keywords: struct, address, pointer
  gcc merges two address computations the original keeps separate: force a pointer local (or the reverse) rather than rewriting the expression.
- id: k-two-use-offset-zero-is-scalar-vs-aggregate | keywords: struct, address, cse, la, scalar, aggregate, offset, load-store
  **A TWO-USE SYMBOL+0 LOAD/STORE PAIR IS EMITTED BARE (`lui $r,%hi / lw $r,%lo($r)` plus `lui $at / sw %lo($at)`, 2 insns each) ONLY WHEN THE OBJECT IS TYPED AS A SCALAR; EVERY AGGREGATE SPELLING MAKES cse MATERIALISE THE ADDRESS (`lui+addiu` la, then `lw 0($r)`/`sw 0($r)`), which also takes a register the rest of the body wanted** (round 431, measured with cc1_probe on field/main/misc2 under BOTH gcc-2.7.2 -G0 and gcc-2.6.0: `extern int gA; v=gA; if(v>0) gA=v+1;` is bare/bare, while the same body on `extern Vec gE; gE.vx` - and on `*(int*)&gE`, `((int*)&gE)[0]`, an `int arr[1]` element, a 4-byte struct member, a single-use pointer local, two pointer locals, the compound assignment, and a volatile load or store - is all `la`+`0($r)`). The offsets are unaffected: `sym+4`/`sym+8` share an la either way, so only the +0 site of an aggregate can show the residual, and it costs one register (typical symptom: the mult/mflo temps shift by one and 30-odd diff lines follow). Consequence for a park: retail's bare/bare pair at offset 0 proves the ORIGINAL accessed that word through a scalar-typed name, so a body using the aggregate member can never reproduce it; measured on field/main/misc2 func_80072D74 (parked 99.06% / 1212 bytes), where spelling the pair through a scalar symbol at g_CameraEye's address made the instruction stream identical (1212 bytes, only the reloc name differing, 99.93). In a TU that already declares the aggregate that scalar declaration is a conflicting-type error (cc1 exit 33; a block-scope shadow errors too), so the remaining step is a data decision - an aliased scalar symbol at the vector's address - not a spelling.
- id: k-original-has-addiu-d | keywords: struct, address, addiu, pointer, lui, loop
  Original has `addiu $d,$s,-K` off a base register where yours re-forms the address with lui/addiu: gcc only keeps the single addiu when the base is a RUNTIME value - a parameter, a pointer loaded from a global, a loop-carried pointer that advances, or a struct-member address reached through a runtime pointer. A compile-time symbol base always folds into a fresh lui/addiu pair.
- id: k-scalar-addr-wall-round | keywords: struct, address, addiu, lui, lw, s32
  SCALAR_ADDR IS NOT A WALL (round 37, battle/misc16_r2): a plain 's32 *pMode = &D_800CCB34;' local dereferenced as '*pMode' DOES emit retail's 'lui sN,%hi / addiu sN,sN,%lo' + 'lw v1,0(sN)' per use; it is not folded away. Four park notes in that unit claim it is - they are wrong. Before accepting a SCALAR_ADDR park, add that local and re-diff.
- id: k-array-extern-fix-address | keywords: struct, address, extern, array, lui, lw, addu, case
  THE ARRAY-EXTERN IS THE FIX FOR THE ADDRESS-OF-GLOBAL FAMILY, SCALARS INCLUDED (rounds 286 and 289: four matches and four parks closed on it). Declare the global as an unbounded ARRAY of its own type and reach it through element 0. For a POINTER global: 'extern u_char* g_X[];' then 'g_X[0] = p;' / 'p = g_X[i];'. For a SCALAR: 'extern u8 g_X[];' then '((s32 *)g_X)[0]' or 'g_X[0]'. That materialises the address in a general register - retail's 'la $s0,g_X' once plus 'lw 0(s0)'/'lbu 0(s0)' per use - where the scalar declaration rematerialises 'lui/lw %hi/%lo' at every site (maspsx's 'lui $at / sw %lo(sym)($at)' macro in the store case). MEASURED: battle/misc17 func_8009209C 67.79 -> 100.00, battle/misc2 BattleAddPrimPair320/280 79.68 -> 100.00 (and a parked sibling closed for free with the same two levers), world_map/misc9 func_80085FE0 92.27 -> 100.00 and func_800865A0 53.39 -> 100.00, world_map/misc1 func_8007299C 98.37 -> 100.00. It is NOT universal: world_map/misc1 func_80071B9C got WORSE (92.11 vs 96.74), and func_8008615C needs the unsplit 'lui $at,%hi; addu $at,$at,v0' form no cc1 here emits - so re-measure, and three other spellings already measured dead for this family: a u_char **pp local in both its init and assigned forms, and a mem_addr dereferenced as *(u_char**)pp. A file-local array-extern for a global a header declares as a scalar is safe while only this unit uses the spelling (the linked bytes are identical) - say so in the park note. If a park note blames this exact shape, try this spelling first.
- id: k-array-extern-is-a-declaration | keywords: struct, address, extern, array, pointer, declaration, shared
  **The array-extern is a DECLARATION-level lever, so a global other functions in the same unit use as a SCALAR cannot take it** (round 392, slus_006.64/psyq/libgpu_c func_80046F30). Re-declaring the shared port pointer `extern volatile u_long *D_800569A4[];` (to try to move the pointer pseudo out of $a2 and into retail's $v1) does not compile: the already-matched func_80046DB4 and func_8004709C read `*D_800569A4 & 0x1000`, which becomes `array & int` - cc1 reports `invalid operands to binary &` twice each, and diff_func.sh prints COMPILE FAILED with the stale-object line. So check the other users of a global before reaching for this lever, and if the whole unit would have to change, the spelling is out.
- id: k-four-more-measured-spelling | keywords: struct, address, loop, u16, extern, frame, addiu, array
  FOUR MORE MEASURED SPELLING LEVERS (round 301). (a) The array-extern works on an unmasked integer COUNTER, not just an address: 'extern u16 g_Counter[];' + 'g_Counter[0]++' makes retail's 'lui/addiu into a register, store through it' appear where the scalar gives the bare 'sw sym' macro (battling/misc2 92.66 -> 94.25). (b) A two-arm 'if (side == 2) mode = 2; else mode = 4;' steals the 'li a0,4' into the inner 'bne' delay slot; a separate 'mode = 4;' statement does not (97.61 -> 98.81). (c) An 'if (count != 0)' wrapper around a do-while KILLS loop inversion - if retail has the rotated loop, the wrapper must not be there. (d) 8-byte locals are load-bearing for a frame: 'u16 localAngle[2]' plus 'long oldGeomOffset[2]'/ 'long pFlag[2]' reproduce retail's 0xA0 frame where plain scalars give 0x98, and a plain u16 local gets hoisted by loop.c (93.45).
- id: k-type-battle-register-file | keywords: struct, address, addu, relocation, u16, u8
  TYPE THE BATTLE REGISTER FILE (round 305, SEVEN functions in one lane). If a park spells the slot register file untyped - (u16 *)(g_BattleSlotWordRegs + index * 0x40), ((u8*)g_BattleSlotRegs)[idx * 0x40 + 0x2E], casts with a +0x10/+0x20/+0x30 offset - write the typed bank instead: g_BattleSlotRegs[index].wordRegs[...], .dwordRegs[...], .byteRegs[...]. retail's base register IS the bank symbol (g_BattleSlotWordRegs is the +0x20 bank), so the typed spelling reproduces the instruction stream exactly and only the relocation addend folds (99.x RELOC, check_bin proves the bytes). battle/misc7 went from ten parks to three in one pass with this: BattleWordTableLoadReg, BattleDwordRegSetReg (its old "addu-order park" was purely the untyped spelling), BattleWordRegSetFromByteReg, BattleStoreCommandBytes, BattleByteRegCopyToMatchedSlot (81.15), BattleDwordRegSetFromSlotArgs (96.17), BattleSlotArgsStoreDwordFromReg (88.95). The same spelling is worth re-measuring in battle/misc8, misc10 and misc6_r1, which already declare BattleSlotRow / BattleSlotRegs / BattleSlotWordRegs views.
- id: k-array-base-pointer-local | keywords: struct, address, la, array, addiu, addu, sll, hoist
  **A POINTER LOCAL FOR THE ARRAY BASE IS WHAT PUTS THE `la` BEFORE THE INDEX MULTIPLY.** round 389, world_map/misc13 func_80092FD8: retail's tail materialises `lui $a0,%hi / addiu $a0,%lo` for the array base BEFORE the `sll/addu/sll` chain that scales the index by the element size; writing `&D_8009D2B8[g_WorldMapPrimPacketIndex]` inline emits the base AFTER the chain instead (95.17%, same 472 bytes, same 118 instructions - only the position of the 2 address words and so the register assignment differs). `pPrims = D_8009D2B8;` as its own statement, then `pPrim = &pPrims[g_WorldMapPrimPacketIndex];`, puts the base exactly where retail has it: 98.52%. It does NOT break the array decay (unlike `&D_8009D2B8`, which folds into the maspsx `$at` macro): the address still reaches a general register. Worth trying whenever the only residual is where the two symbol-address words sit in a store group; the rest of the mismatch that survives it is a GIV_TIE on the six argument registers.

- id: a-shift-spelling-beats-times-four | keywords: lui, lo, displacement, fold, shift, andi, address, combine
  **WRITE SCALING AS `<< N`, NEVER `* N`: with `(x + 0x10) * 4` gcc 2.7.2's combine distributes the mul over the add and folds the constant into the `%lo` displacement (`lbu v0,64(at)`), where `(x + 0x10) << 2` keeps retail's `addiu v0,v1,0x10 / sll v0,v0,2` and a bare `lbu %lo(sym)(at)`** (round 399, battle/main/misc13 func_80088490 66.00 -> 75.25, and the matched neighbour BattleIsCharSlot18InUse uses the shift spelling). Proved on a 6-line replica with `cc1_probe.sh`: the two spellings differ by exactly those two instructions. Any diff whose original has `addiu $r,$r,N / sll $r,$r,M` before a `%hi/%lo` symbol access while the compile has the same access with a folded displacement is this lever.
- id: a-byte-offset-in-a-local-keeps-at | keywords: address, la, base, register, offset, lui, lo, at
  **A BYTE OFFSET SPELLED INLINE AT EACH SITE (`g_Table[(u8)arg0 * 0x1C]` five times) MAKES cc1 MATERIALISE `la $v1,g_Table` AND REUSE ONE BASE REGISTER; holding it in an `s32 ofs` LOCAL keeps retail's per-access `lui at,%hi / addu at,at,ofs / op %lo(at)` form** (round 399, battle/main/misc13 func_80088490 75.25 -> 86.50, reproduced in `cc1_probe.sh` on the two spellings side by side). So when retail re-forms `$at` for every access to one symbol and the compile emits a single `la` base, add the offset local rather than looking for a pointer form; the reverse move (local -> inline) is what creates the `la`.
- id: k-three-s32-externs-for | keywords: struct, vector, extern, s32, cast, scratch, gte, la, twin
  **THREE SEPARATE s32 EXTERNS FOR ONE 3-WORD GTE SCRATCH VECTOR ARE WHAT PINS THE ADDRESSES IN THE PROLOGUE: declare the globals as `VECTOR` and use fields** (round 399, battling/main/misc12 func_800898BC, parked 50.18% -> MATCH / 98.99 RELOC at retail's exact 476 bytes / 119 insns, preset XenoBattlingNoGp -G0, one run). Retail keeps `&D_8009A0C8` / `&D_8009A918` / `&D_80096F98` / `&D_80097000` in `$s2`/`$s3`/`$s4`/`$s0` across five `jal`s and stores `D_8009A0C8.vx` through `$s2`; with twelve `extern s32 D_8009A0CC;`-style scalars plus `(VECTOR*)&D_8009A0C8` casts the same statements hoist every `lui/addiu` into the prologue, store through `$at` where retail stores through `$s2`, batch the `lh` pairs, and score 50.18% (464 bytes), 57.30% with the array-extern trick - so the "address materialisation is hoisted / $at vs saved register" residual was the CAST spelling, not the allocator and not the preset (no need for try_flags.sh). The three scratch vectors are 0x10 apart (0x80096F98 -> g_BattleViewOrigin at +0x10, 0x80097000 -> g_BattleActor0 at +0x10, 0x8009A918 -> g_BattleTargetCrosses at +0x10, 0x8009A0C8 -> g_BattleDrawBuffers at +0x10), i.e. real VECTORs including the pad word, so one `extern VECTOR` per triple and `.vx/.vy/.vz` access is both realistic and exact; the +4/+8 field reads name separate `D_` symbols in the expected object, so objdiff stops at 98.99 RELOC and check_bin proves the bytes. Companion find: `twin_for.py` said "no twin", but grepping the CALL SEQUENCE (`VectorNormal` twice at the head/tail of two `OuterProduct12`s feeding a MATRIX) found battle/main/misc31's BattleBuildMatrixFromThreeVectors - the same function with STACK locals instead of these globals, and that one difference is the whole residual. Grep the call sequence when twin_for reports none.

- id: a-address-of-a-global-assigned | keywords: la, lui, addiu, address, join, label, cse, twice
  **A CALLEE-SAVED `la` OF A GLOBAL THAT RETAIL RE-FORMS AT A BRANCH-JOIN IS NOT OBTAINABLE FROM ONE POINTER LOCAL: assign the pointer a SECOND time where the re-form belongs.** battle/main/misc16_r2 func_800904A0 (round 400, gcc-2.6.0) needs `lui/addiu &g_BattlePrimStartIndex` at 0x560 and again at 0x624, the join of the first palette if/else: one `pMode = &g` before the first half keeps the address in the same register across the whole body and the function is exactly 2 instructions short (612 against retail's 620 bytes), while a second `pMode = &g;` statement at the start of the second half cannot be coalesced away because cse's table is reset at that label, so it re-emits the `la` into the same register and the size becomes retail's. The same unit's matched BattleDrawMenuEntryString shows the counter-spelling: `g_BattlePrimStartIndex[0]` folds to the maspsx `lw $v1,%lo(sym)` (no `la`), so an array-index spelling can never produce retail's `la` + `lw 0(reg)` pair.
- id: i-an-inline-table-index-beats | keywords: switch, sll, duplicate, reorg, delay, ofs, local, u8
  **A MULTIPLY THAT reorg COPIES INTO EVERY SWITCH DELAY SLOT IS A LOCAL THAT SHOULD BE AN INLINE INDEX:** battle/main/misc16_r2 func_800904A0 kept `ofs = row * 4;` in a local, and reorg stole the `sll` into all three switch dispatch slots (3 copies, 153 insns / 612 bytes); writing the index inline at each use (`D_800D2F6A[row * 4]`) with a `u8` column variable gives one `sll` after the switch and 155 insns = retail's size (73.99 -> 79.85). The `u8` column also reproduces retail's `andi s0,s2,0xFF` before the second half's `sll`, which an `s32` column with `& 0xFF` at the use site does not.
- id: k-two-globals-need-two-address-spellings | keywords: struct, address, array, extern, volatile, reload, cse, lui
  **TWO GLOBALS READ IN THE SAME COMPARE CAN NEED OPPOSITE ADDRESS SPELLINGS, and a blanket `volatile` on the array extern is what breaks the pair** (round 412, world_map/main/misc3 func_80076FA8, 75.16 -> 100.00 at retail's exact 140 insns / 560 bytes, one lane, preset XenoWorldMap). Retail keeps `&g_WorldMapScrollTargetX` in `$s2` across the `WorldMapWrapDelta` jal and reaches it as `lw a2,0(s2)` / `lw a0,0(s2)` while re-using the compare's value for the x delta, yet RELOADS `g_WorldMapScrollTargetY` and `...Z` (fresh `lui v0 / lw v0`) after the `sw` to the delta struct. The three fixes measured here, in order: (1) the two scroll writes `g_WorldMapScrollX += d` / `g_WorldMapScrollZ += d` came out as the maspsx `lui $at / sw %lo($at)` form until they were spelled through the non-volatile array alias (`extern s32 g_WorldMapScrollXTable[] __asm__("g_WorldMapScrollX"); ... g_WorldMapScrollXTable[0] += pDelta->x;`, the k-array-extern-fix-address lever) - that alone took the body 75.16 -> 90.34 and is 10 of the 12 missing instructions; (2) TargetX wants the SAME array alias (non-volatile, so the store's reuse of the compare value survives and the address takes `$s2`) - without it the two reads emit `lui/lw` pairs and the body is 2 instructions short at 92.06 with the beqz delay slot holding the arg setup instead of the `subu`; (3) TargetY/Z want plain `volatile` scalars so each read is a real load - `extern volatile s32 g_WorldMapScrollTargetY;` (a sub-word volatile would have cost an `andi`, a word one does not). Spelling (2) with `volatile` too (the tempting blanket form) gives 142 insns because the `$s2` decay dies exactly as the k-four-more-measured-spelling note says. So on a compare whose values retail half-reuses, half-reloads: measure the array alias per global, and put `volatile` on the per-read ones only.

- id: k-type-the-battle-queue-instead-of-a-pointer-local | keywords: struct, pointer, local, cast, battlecommandqueue, switch, cross-jump, park
  **A BATTLE-MENU PARK THAT SPELLS `g_BattleCommandQueue` AS A `u8 *p` POINTER LOCAL (`off = a * 0x40; p[off + 0x3C]`) LOSES BOTH THE BASE REGISTER AND GCC'S CROSS-JUMPING - the typed `((BattleActorSlots *)g_BattleCommandQueue)->slot[a].field` form is retail's own spelling and reaches its exact byte count** (round 417, battle/main/misc11_b func_8008115C 88.35% park -> MATCH at retail's 444 bytes, and func_800820A4 96.88% / 552 bytes -> MATCH at retail's 544 bytes, both 99.9x JTBL-only, one edit each). Retail reloads the queue with `lui/lw` per case and reuses that ONE register for the flags load, the targetSlot compare and the state store (`lhu 0x32(v0)` then `sb v0,0x2DD(v1)`); the pointer-local body pinned the base in `$a1` across the whole case body. It also defeated the tail merge: retail's case-2 and case-3 `menuState = N; repeatPending = 0;` tails share one `sb v0,0x2DD(a0)` block with the two different `li v0,N` constants sitting in the preceding branches' delay slots (reorg then jump.c), which only happens when each arm's store is built from its own inline struct access - the pointer local made every arm reach the store through one live pseudo, so gcc emitted a `j` plus delay slot instead and cost 8 bytes. Corollary: two parks in the same family that both blame "cross-jump / block placement" and "the base landing in $5" are one spelling bug, and the neighbours already matched show the fix - BattleAdvanceSlotMenuStateEventSubMode/Activate/Random in the same file were ported from twins in the struct form and sat at 99.9x JTBL-only. Try the typed form before lever.py on any `u8 *`-typed queue park.

- id: k-name-packet-words-as-an-array-not-as-symbols | keywords: struct, array, extern, symbol, addend, reloc, objdiff, packet, bss
  **A STATIC PACKET BUFFER WHOSE WORDS SPLAT LABELLED SEPARATELY WANTS ONE `extern u_long D_X[N]` AND AN INDEX, NOT N SCALAR EXTERNS: the scalar spelling changes the register set and the size, while the array spelling matches and only objdiff's `D_X+0x10`-vs-`D_x48` addend differs** (round 421, slus_006.64/psyq/libgpu_b func_80045E44, MATCH at retail's exact 151 insns / 604 bytes and `check_bin.sh slus_006.64` OK, objdiff 98.94% on reloc addends only). The .bss dump lists D_8005A238..D_8005A268 as one `.space 4` each, so it is tempting to declare 13 scalars and get retail's `lui $at,%hi(D_8005A248)` operands verbatim - that body compiles to 152 insns / 608 bytes: the `&D_8005A25C` address pseudo disappears (so `func_800465EC` gets `lw a0,0(a0)` instead of `addiu a0`), the rect parameter moves from `$t0` to `$a3` and 53 lines differ. The array form (`extern u_long D_8005A238[9]` + a second `extern u_long D_8005A25C[4]` node) matches, and the only objdiff deltas are the folded addends, which is the documented reloc exception - report it MATCH/RELOC and gate with check_bin. Two spellings that keep it matching: the linked-list tag as `0x08000000 | ((u_long)D_8005A25C & 0xFFFFFF)` (an array name decays to an `la` of the symbol, so the tag and the terminator store share one address register, as retail has them), and `*(u_long *)&rect->x` for the packed RECT corners.

- id: k-a-global-pointer-member-read-inline-reloads-its-base | keywords: struct, reload, lui, callee-saved, pointer, local, frame, cse
  **A `g_Menu->pMember[...]` REACHED THROUGH A LOCAL (`p = g_Menu->pMember;` then `p[i]`) KEEPS THE BASE IN A CALLEE-SAVED REGISTER AND IS SHORT ~2-4 INSTRUCTIONS PER USE, WHILE THE SAME ACCESS SPELLED INLINE AT EVERY USE REPRODUCES RETAIL'S `lui %hi(g_Menu) / lw / lw 0x434` RELOAD - and retail's saved-register set and frame size are the evidence of which shape the original had** (round 434, menu/main/misc9 func_801DE5CC, a 706-insn redo park, 79.90 -> 92.30 in four measured steps). Two spellings carried the whole 29-instruction gap: (1) the eight-name render loop wrote `g_Menu->pCharacterSpriteRecords->unk0[i].width = ...` inline at seven sites that each follow a call, and retail reloads `g_Menu` and its `0x434` member after every one of those calls (79.90 -> 86.81, 645 -> 676 insns); a `pSpriteRecords` local held the address in `$s2` across all the calls and could not be reached from any other spelling. (2) The four-arm page select read `((u8*)g_Menu->pEquipmentPanel)[...]` inline, so retail reloads `g_Menu` in each arm (86.81 -> 89.49); a hoisted `pPanel` made gcc load `g_Menu` once before the `beqz` that opens the dispatch. Corollary for a park note that blames "the frame is 0x10 small" or "retail keeps X live in $fp": count how many times retail re-forms the same base address, and if it does, the local is the bug - gcc will not un-CSE a local back into per-use loads.

- id: k-a-row-pointer-local-buys-the-displacement-and-costs-the-allocation | keywords: displacement, addiu, lo16, fold, pointer, local, symbol, offset, residual
  **WHEN THE ONLY SHAPE DIFFERENCE IS WHICH REGISTER A SYMBOL'S +0x14E LANDS IN - retail `addiu v1,v1,%lo(g_X)` then `lhu a0,0x14E(v0)`, ours `addiu v1,v1,%lo(g_X)+0x14E` then `lhu a0,0(v0)` - A POINTER LOCAL TO THE ROW BUYS THE DISPLACEMENT FORM AND PAYS FOR IT WITH THE WHOLE ALLOCATION** (round 437, battle/main/misc33 func_800BF3E8, XenoBattleTailCdk0, still parked at 85.91). The plain expression `*(u16 *)(g_BattleStateWork + g_BattleCommandRowIndex * 0x48 + 0x14E)` folds the offset into the symbol addiu, because a symbol+const is materialised as one `lui`/`addiu` pair before the index is added; `pWork = g_BattleStateWork + g_BattleCommandRowIndex * 0x48;` with `*(u16 *)(pWork + 0x14E)` gives gcc a REGISTER base with addend 0, so 0x14E becomes the HImode load's displacement exactly as retail has it - and it also moves the index multiply into the other register (`v1` instead of `v0`), hands pState `$a0` instead of `$a1`, and (with a plain second read of the same global) lets cse drop the reload entirely: 85.91 -> 76.32 at the same 264 bytes, and 71.50 / 252 bytes for the non-volatile variant. So measure the pointer local once when the residual is the fold, then keep whichever body wins on the instruction stream - the fold alone is not worth the allocation.

- id: k-a-negative-index-offset-needs-a-pointer-local-to-stay-a-displacement | keywords: pointer, local, displacement, fold, symbol, negative, index, lo16
  **`sym[index - k]` FOLDS THE -k INTO A SECOND SYMBOL ADDRESS (`lui/addiu` of `sym - 2k` and a `lhu 0($base)`), WHILE A POINTER LOCAL NAMING THE MIDDLE OF THE TABLE KEEPS ONE BASE AND MAKES THE OFFSET A LOAD DISPLACEMENT - the same +4 bytes per extra base that k-a-row-pointer-local-buys-the-displacement names, seen from the negative side** (round 440, field/main/misc5 func_800A8BA4, Default preset: 800 -> 784 bytes / 200 -> 196 insns against retail's 772 / 194, still parked at 85.21%). Retail reaches a `u_short` rect table as one hoisted base `s5 = 0x800AEB6C` plus `lhu t1,0(a2)` / `lhu t2,2(a3)` / `lhu a3,-4(t0)` (w, h, and the record's x at -4); writing `D_800AEB6C[nRect]`, `[nRect + 1]`, `[nRect - 2]` makes gcc emit a `lui/addiu` for 0x800AEB68 and a zero-displacement load there, and the fourth field's symbol comes out through `$at`. Declaring `u_short *pRect = D_800AEB6C;` once before the loop and indexing `pRect[nRect]`, `pRect[nRect + 1]`, `pRect[nRect - 2]` gives retail's single base and the -4 displacement (the fourth, positive-offset field keeps its own symbol, as retail's does), and it also frees the register pressure so the base gets hoisted. So when a diff shows retail indexing one symbol at a negative displacement and yours at a second symbol with displacement 0, spell the table through a pointer local - the price this lever's sibling measures (a changed allocation) is not paid here because the pointer replaces a base the loop needed anyway.


- id: k-a-row-read-constant-lands-in-the-symbol-addend | keywords: addend, symbol, fold, displacement, lhu, row, split, statement
  **A `global + idx * stride + CONST` LOAD PUTS THE CONSTANT IN THE SYMBOL'S `%lo` ADDEND, WHERE RETAIL'S SHAPE HAS A 0 ADDEND AND THE CONSTANT IN THE LOAD DISPLACEMENT - and in gcc 2.7.2-cdk only a statement split moves it out, which always costs more than the fold** (round 440, battle/main/misc30 func_800B9C78, re-parked 98.93% at retail's exact 768 bytes / 192 insns, XenoBattleTailCdk0 = gcc-2.7.2-cdk -G0). Retail's five row reads are `lui v1,%hi(g_BattleStateWork) / addiu v1,v1,%lo(g_BattleStateWork)` (no addend) then `addu v0,<idx*0x48>,v1` (destination on the index sum) and `lhu a1,0x14E(v0)`; the one-expression `*(u16 *)(g_BattleStateWork + g_BattleCommandRowIndex * 0x48 + 0x14E)` emits the identical instruction count and addu order but folds the constant: `addiu v1,v1,0x14E / lhu a1,0(v0)`. That single residual is 10 of the body's ~16 differing lines. Both splits are measured and worse: a `u8 *pRow = g_BattleStateWork + g_BattleCommandRowIndex * 0x48;` local in ONE arm gives 96.35 (the fix works, but the arm's load schedule rotates and the base then CSEs into the function's saved `$s4`, which retail re-forms), and the same local at all five sites gives 74.36 at 816 bytes because the shared local becomes callee-saved (+12 instructions). A typed row gives no way out either: these rows are read at ABSOLUTE block offsets 0x14E/0x15B/0x172/0x17F while their stride is 0x48, so no element type both holds those fields and strides 0x48 (misc29/misc32 reach the same block through a 0x180-byte view with an explicit `* 0x48`). Companion, also measured: declaration order does not move the callee-saved hand-out there (declaring the `u8 code` before the `s32 index` measures the identical 98.93), and one 3-minute permuter run (1511 iterations, base 200 best 170) found only ONE arm's split, spelled by reusing a live local as the row temp (`(slot = idx * 0x48)`), which is wrong code and still far from matching. So: when the ONLY residual is one folded constant per site, park on it - and check the park's jump table against the `.s` first, because this body's parked C was missing a `case` (its 13-word table put the default in slot 5 where retail's `jtbl_80070A80` has the func_800B9284 arm, and slot 6 is the default).

- id: k-a-plus2-field-displacement-off-a-live-base-is-cdk-only | keywords: displacement, fold, base, pointer, alias, asm, label, cdk, halfword, probe
  **A `base + 2` HALFWORD REF OFF A LIVE SYMBOL-BASE POINTER IS FOLDED BY cc1 2.6.0 AND 2.7.2 INTO A BARE `%lo(sym+2)` ACCESS, SO A RETAIL BODY WITH `lh 2(s5)` / `lhu 2(s5)` (the SECOND field of a vector read through one `la`) IS ONLY REACHABLE FROM THE cdk cc1 - and an offset-0 ref off the same base IS kept, so the diff shows one register form and one folded form side by side** (round 441, battle/main/misc25 func_800B0B14, re-parked 96.47% at 153 insns / 612 bytes against retail's 151 / 604, XenoBattle260Div; the two A.vy sites are the entire 2-instruction gap). Proved with `cc1_probe.sh battle/main/misc25` on a 12-line snippet (`extern unsigned short g_A[] __asm__("g_A"); p = g_A; a = p[0] + p[1]; b = g(); return a + p[0] + p[1] + b;`): 2.6.0 and 2.7.2 emit `la $17,g_A` then `lhu $16,0($17)` but `lhu $2,g_A+2` at BOTH +2 sites, while cdk emits `lhu $2,2($18)` / `lhu $4,%lo(g_A)($17)`. So do not spend diff runs on the spelling; score the whole body under `--gcc gcc-2.7.2-cdk-psx` (here 97.36) and check `try_flags.sh <unit> --all --gcc gcc-2.7.2-cdk-psx` before believing it, because cdk also drops that unit's matched body (BattleComputeScaledSine 88.95, BattleIsPointWithinBounds 79.38), i.e. the function's original TU was not the unit's compiler. Companion measured on the same body: the address only reaches a callee-saved register at all through the asm-label array alias (`extern BattleSlotVectorWords g_BattleSlotVectorWords[] __asm__("g_BattleSlotVectors"); pVectors = g_BattleSlotVectorWords;`, 96.47), where a scalar `&g_BattleSlotVectors`, a plain pointer local, a `u16 *` cast and a `mem_addr` assignment all fold it away (93.07-93.27); a `volatile` pointer does not stop the +2 fold (probed); and a struct-typed alias with `u16 vx` / `s16 vy` fields compiles byte-identically to the `u16 *` plus `(s16)` cast spelling, which is what keeps the `lhu`/`lh` widths retail has.

- id: k-a-block-field-view-beats-a-raw-offset-pointer-local | keywords: struct, field, view, block, offset, displacement, base, hoist
  **A CONSTANT BLOCK OFFSET (`gfxWork + 0x63D0`) WRITTEN AS A RAW POINTER LOCAL IS FOLDED INTO A REGISTER BY cc1 (`addu $7,$10,25552` hoisted, stores at `0(reg)`), WHILE THE SAME OFFSET AS A STRUCT FIELD (`((Block *)gfxWork)->rows[idx].x0`, rows at 0x63D0) leaves it as the STORE DISPLACEMENT and puts `gfxWork` in the base register, which is retail's shape at all 8 stores** (round 444, battle/main/misc1 func_80071B94, XenoBattle260 = gcc 2.6.0, parked 81.13% / 1724 bytes / 431 insns against retail 1756 / 439). It also fixed the stride: the row struct must be the ARRAY stride (0x18) and not the size of the eight vertex words it holds (0x10), otherwise every address is `sll idx,4` instead of retail's `sll/addu/sll` *24. Both edits together moved 79.59 -> 81.05 with no change to the instruction gaps elsewhere, i.e. an address-shape lever, not a score lever. Companion measured find in the same body: eight `sh` stores whose prim index retail RELOADS before each store need `*(volatile s32 *)&g` at each of the eight index sites (79.85 with the plain spelling) because the volatile DECLARATION is shared with a neighbour that drops to 99.45.

## GTE macros

symptoms: gte_ macro words, nops or clobbers differ
try: gte macro cop2
- id: l-gte-x-macro-whose | keywords: gte, macro
  **A `gte_*` 3.x macro whose body is a bare `.word 0x0000xxxx` is a DMPSX PSEUDO WORD that maspsx does not expand** (round 382, battle/misc26): four `gte_avsz3()` sites emitted `.word 0x000011BF` where retail has the expanded command `0x4B58002D`, and spelling them `gte_nop(); gte_nop(); gte_avsz3_b();` (the psyq spelling) took the park 97.13 -> 97.45% at the same byte count. `gte_nclip`/`gte_rtpt` beside them were fine because their 3.x forms are written as `c2 0x...`. So when a GTE stub's diff shows a two-word constant where retail has one command word, check the header's macro body for a `.word` before blaming the allocator - maspsx never expands a hand-written `.word`.
- id: l-gte-macros-include-psyq | keywords: gte, macro, cop2, nop
  The GTE macros in include/psyq/inline_c.h are the RIGHT SDK revision (3.x: one nop plus the canonical COP2 word; read-backs into $2/$8 via gte_*_gpr and gte_*_val). NormalColor, ColorCol, LocalLight, Square*, SetRot/Light/ColorMatrix, AverageSZ3/Z4 all match. Still blocked, do not retry blind: the *3 family (NormalColor3, ColorCol3, DpqColor3) where retail lazily loads incoming stack arguments after the GTE command, and functions whose .s is bare mtc2/ctc2 with no macro equivalent.
- id: l-macro-whose-scratch-registers | keywords: gte, macro
  A macro whose scratch registers are declared as hard-coded clobbers ("$2","$3") beats "=r" outputs: that is what makes gcc copy a1/a2 into $v0 at the end instead of storing through $v0. If a GTE store/read-back is one register off, try the clobber form.
- id: l-gte-body-convertible-unless | keywords: gte, macro
  A GTE BODY IS CONVERTIBLE UNLESS ITS .s SAYS 'Handwritten function'. splat prints that marker for the functions the original studio wrote as assembly (all 19 GTE stubs of slus_006.64/system/model and several of system/temp2 carry it), and those can never be C. A stub with mtc2/lwc2/swc2 and NO marker is ordinary compiler output that the inline_c.h macros express - battle/misc35 func_800C0D18 is 146 instructions at 100.00% through gte_lddp/gte_ldlvl/gte_gpf12/gte_stlvl after an earlier lane called it un-convertible from the disassembly alone. So read the marker before you write a body off, and check that the park includes psyq/inline_c.h (without it every GTE site compiles as a CALL and the body measures nothing).

## park hygiene and stale notes

symptoms: park note blames compiler, stale body, size mismatch
try: park stale note include_asm
- id: m-park-py-glues-and-swallows | keywords: park, unpark, repark, comment, swallowed, check_bin, undefined, reference
  **`tools/scripts/park.py` CONSUMES THE NEWLINE AFTER THE PARK'S `*/`, SO `unpark` LEAVES `}` AND THE NEXT DEFINITION GLUED ON ONE LINE (`}void Next(...)`), AND `repark` THEN TAKES THAT WHOLE FUNCTION INTO THE PARK COMMENT** (round 420, battle/main/misc2 func_80073B64: re-parking it swallowed the already-matched `BattleLoadImageAndSync` that sat under the stub, and the only symptom was `undefined reference to 'BattleLoadImageAndSync'` from two OTHER units at matching-mode link - lint_sources.py was clean and the objdiff diff of the function itself was 89.56%). The DEF regex ends on a line-start `}` and the glue hides it, so after ANY unpark look for `}void` in the file, and after ANY repark grep that the functions which followed the stub are still live code, then run `check_bin.sh <bin>`. Fix is one edit: put the `*/` + `INCLUDE_ASM` line between the two definitions.
- id: m-repark-rewrites-the-include-asm-path | keywords: park, repark, include_asm, path, nonmatchings, undefined, link
  **`park.py repark` WRITES THE STUB BACK AS `INCLUDE_ASM("asm/<unit>", Func);`, DROPPING THE BINARY PREFIX AND THE `nonmatchings/` LEVEL, so the re-parked function has no asm to include and the overlay fails to link** (round 421, battle/main/misc2 func_80073B64 and func_80076710, whose real target is `asm/battle/nonmatchings/main/misc2`): the instrument rewritten the whole line from the unit tag it was given (`misc2`), not from the stub that was already there. Nothing in the diff of the parked body shows it, objdiff is silent because the function is a stub again, and `lint_sources.py` only counts INCLUDE_ASM lines so it passes. After every repark, `grep -n 'INCLUDE_ASM("asm/' <file>` and compare against the file's other stubs; fix the paths to the neighbours' spelling before the gate. `check_bin.sh <bin>` catches it.
- id: m-a-park-measured-on-a-park | keywords: park, stale, note, compile, offset, maspsx, displacement, at
  **A PARK IN THIS REPO CAN BE UNBUILDABLE AND STILL CARRY A PERCENTAGE: re-unpark, fix the compile errors and re-measure before reading the note as fact** (round 420, battle/main/misc2 func_80073B64 parked "86.09%", whose body indexed `g_BattleDisplayState[0x97]` after that global became a `BattleWork *` - cc1 exit 33, no score at all - and whose four atlas byte offsets were wrong by 0x10, see the disclosure lever under struct/typed spelling). The same function's other park (func_80076710) re-measured to its note's number exactly (97.94% / 724 bytes), so re-measuring costs one diff run and tells you which notes are real.
- id: m-an-old-permuter-find-costs | keywords: park, stale, permuter, do-while, wrapper, switch, tie
  **AN OLD PERMUTER FIND CAN GO FROM LOAD-BEARING TO COSTLY WHEN THE BODY AROUND IT CHANGES - re-run the permuter from the CURRENT body before believing the note.** battle/main/misc16 func_8008CFB8 was parked at 99.40% with a `do { BattleStepAnimatedCursorPrim(...); } while (0)` wrapper that a round-167 permuter run had found worth +0.05% when the row offset was strength-reduced from 0; once `s32 firstRow;` had moved the biv to start at retail's 0x5C8, the same wrapper was what kept `cursor` out of $s3 and the codes walk out of $s2. Deleting it left every one of the other 219 instructions untouched and closed the function at 100.00% (880 bytes, base=110 -> best=0 in 666 iterations, and that single line was the permuter's only edit). Two lessons: a park note's "permuter found X" is a fact about the body it ran on, not about the function, so one 3-minute re-run from the current body is cheap insurance; and when a park's whole residual is a callee-saved SWAP, check the allocation nudges already in the note (empty/do-while blocks, statement wrappers) for one that is no longer needed before reaching for the -dg/-dl dumps.
- id: m-editing-a-park-note-with | keywords: park, note, comment, syntax, build, lint, opener, replace
  **A TEXT REPLACE THAT REWRITES A PARK NOTE CAN DROP ITS `/*` OPENER AND NOTHING SHORT OF A MATCHING BUILD NOTICES.** (round 400, slus_006.64/system/animation_scripts func_800223B0). `park.py repark --note "/* Best: ... */"` writes the note as its own block comment and then the `/* body */` block; a later edit that prefixes descriptive `//` lines by replacing the literal `/* Best:` deletes the only opener, the note becomes live C, and cc1 fails with `syntax error before ','` on the prose (`ONE shared block` -> `4096 shared block` after macro expansion). `lint_sources.py -q` passes (the body's own block is still balanced) and `diff_func.sh` still scores the function, so the only symptom is `make build` / `check_bin.sh` dying with `[code=33] ... .c.i:NNNN: syntax error`, with ninja's message hidden behind `>/dev/null`. When you touch a note, re-read the region and check the note line starts with `/*` or `//`, or write the note as `//` lines only.
- id: m-before-cite-old-park | keywords: park, stale, volatile, jal, frame, cast, gte, li
  **Before you cite an old park note that blames the compiler, check the parked body still COMPILES against today's externs.** A body that calls a callee with the wrong arity never built, so its recorded percentage came from an older variant and the note's diagnosis is fiction. battle/misc25's 99.70% park closed on the first try once the call got the argument its extern declares. A park can also be the wrong CALL SET, not just the wrong shape: battle/misc26's 765-instruction park called BattleEmitDrawTPage in all eight kind arms where retail calls it once and only from the four untextured ones, and fixing that alone moved it 85.07 -> 89.78% (3164 -> 3028 bytes). So when a park's call count does not match the asm's `jal` count for that symbol, fix the calls before you touch a register or a local.n- **A park 5-7 points short with a CYCLIC register rotation is an OVER-ALLOCATION, not a cse cost rule.** battle/misc26 func_800B1F6C sat at 89.78% with the mask 0xFF000000 rematerialised 7 times and two copies of a link tail; freeing ONE callee-saved local changed nothing (86.71%, still 7 li, still 2 jal) but freeing TWO hoisted both masks by itself (0 li, 1 jal, retail's two tails): the body was two registers over, and no spelling of the constant was ever the issue. So count the `.mask`/`.frame` line's saved registers against retail's before you theorise about the constant, drop locals whose live range ends in the preheader, and expect a 7-point jump. **A value retail keeps in a register whose live range does NOT overlap another's can be expressed in source by reusing that local**: retail's saved packet code byte lives in the same $s0 as pV0, so `pV0 = (SVECTOR *)(u32)((P_TAG *)pPacket)->code;` in each unlit arm and `((P_TAG *)pPacket)->code = (u8)(u32)pV0;` afterwards is a register-allocation hint, not a style choice - that one edit plus the two freed registers took the body 89.78 -> 96.80% (3028 -> 2940 bytes against retail's 2944). The `volatile`-pointer trick that forces a shared pseudo through memory is NOT the answer when the frame has no spare slot: retail's 0x88 frame there has all twelve var slots used, and a `volatile` adds an 8-byte one. **When several plain stores have their loads hoisted ABOVE the whole store group, make only the first N-1 of them volatile:** battling/misc2's BattleEffectSetMatrix was stuck at 63.89% because gcc lifted all three lw of the globals above the three stores, and every plain spelling (typed fields, a typed-address cast, a SVECTOR* view, an s16 pos[3] field, even volatile globals) measured the same 63.89 - but marking exactly the FIRST TWO stores with a volatile lvalue cast while the THIRD stays plain gives retail's interleaving at 216 bytes / **100.00%**, because the third store is then free to fill the following jal's delay slot (all three volatile is 95.19 - a move takes the slot instead - and only the middle one is 77.24). A body that silently fails to build scores nothing but reads like a percentage: `diff_func.sh` hides a cc1 error (ninja FAILED, exit 33) and the note then quotes a STALE object - lib_gear's func_801DCEC8 was parked at 87.92% for two rounds on a body that never compiled (`gte_stlvnl(vec)` must be `gte_stlvnl(&vec)`, 'inconsistent operand constraints in an asm' at four sites), and battle/misc21's func_8009F844 lacked `#include "psyq/inline_c.h"` entirely, so all twenty GTE sites compiled as CALLS to gte_ldv0/gte_rtps (61.32 -> 68.70% from the include alone). Before trusting any park, un-park it and watch the COMPILE itself, and if the unit uses GTE macros check that include first.
- id: m-park-run-may-already | keywords: park, stale, sh
  **A park in a 2.6.0 run may already be a match: check the preset before writing C.** round 314 un-parked three `menu/misc7` bodies at 94.00/94.26/94.60 (Default) that are 100.00 under gcc 2.6.0 with no C change at all. `try_flags.sh <unit> --all --gcc gcc-2.6.0-psx` scores every body in one compile, and `--all` includes parks.
- id: m-splat-s-handwritten-function | keywords: park, stale, gte, macro
  **splat's `/* Handwritten function */` marker is NOT authoritative** (round 318): `battle/misc24`'s `func_800AF270` matched as C and the unit's already-matched `BattleSpriteUpdateWorldPosition` carries the marker too. A stub with the marker is not automatically unconvertible - what IS unconvertible is hand-written `mtc2`/`mfc2`/`addi` with no macro equivalent (the 19 GTE stubs of `system/model`), so read the body rather than the marker.
- id: m-park-note-s-byte | keywords: park, stale, cc1, mfhi, u8, addu, s32, array
  **A park note's byte count is testimony, not evidence.** round 370: field/misc8's func_8008492C note claimed retail is 280 bytes and it is 276, so its whole size argument was fiction, and lib_gear's func_801DEF10 was briefed at 98.63 and re-measured 94.95. Re-measure before spending a lane on a note's diagnosis. **The cheap detector for a body that never compiled is `tools/scripts/cc1_probe.sh <unit> <file.c>`** (round 371): `diff_func.sh` exits 33 with NO output when cc1 fails, so a park can carry a percentage from a variant that never built and you will not see why. Round 371 found THREE such notes in one unit (`pFlags = g_BattleDisplayState` assigning a struct pointer to `u8*`, `g_BattlePrimStartIndex * 0x28` on an `s32[]`, `s32 *pPrim = &g_BattlePrimStartIndex`); run the probe right after an unpark and it prints cc1's errors for the whole file in ~10 s. **A missing ARGUMENT shows up as a missing register copy: check the callee's arity before blaming allocation.** round 371, field/misc4 func_8007B814 (96.33 -> 100.00): retail passes a 4th argument (`addu $a3,$zero,$zero` before the `jal`), so giving the prototype a 4th parameter and passing `0` at all three call sites both supplies retail's `move $a3,zero` AND stops cc1 hoisting the shared tail's first `lw` above the `j`. **A Psy-Q prim's packet length byte has no field in `include/psyq/libgpu.h`** (`POLY_FT4.tag` is a plain `u_long`), so `((u8*)&pPrim->tag)[3] = 9;` is the honest spelling - `setlen()` is a whole-word macro that emits `lw/andi/ori/sw` where retail has one `sb`. Keep the cast and report it ROUGH rather than editing the shared header. **THE `mfhi`/`mflo` DIVISION TIE IS A COMPILER DIFFERENCE - do not spell at it.** round 373 proved it with seven `cc1_probe` runs: under **gcc 2.7.2 every spelling** of `x / K` and `x % K` (s32, u32, u16, s16 dividend, one expression or two statements, narrow result local, `(u16)` cast on the dividend) puts `mfhi` in a TEMP register (`mfhi $5` + `sra $4,$5,5`), while **under gcc 2.6.0 the same six spellings emit retail's in-place form** (`mfhi $4` + `sra $4,$4,5`) with no source change at all. So a 2.7.2 unit whose parks are capped by `mfhi`/`mflo` destinations cannot be closed from source: report `PRESET_260 <unit> <Func> <pct 2.6.0> <pct own>` with the scores of every already-matched function and let the orchestrator switch or split the unit. A park note that blames the allocator here was right about the mechanism and wrong about the cause. **Base-first `addu` in a store group needs the sum as a DECLARED value in two statements** (round 374, battle/misc23 func_800A64E4: 15 sites byte-identical): `pRow = gX;` then `pRow = pRow + i * 0x24;` - an inline address always comes out index-first through the giv (ten `cc1_probe` spellings all measured `addu $2,$5,$2`), and each stored colour byte must also have its own `u8` local (`b = pSrc[0]; pRow[0x1740] = b;`) or the byte load takes `$v0` and the base `$v1`, the mirror of retail. **Array DECAY beats the address-of-global fold** (round 374, world_map/misc1: nothing else was left): `pQueue = g_WorldMapStreamQueue;` with an array extern emits retail's `la`, while `pQueue = &g_WorldMapStreamQueue;` - and `(void*)`, `(u32)`, `&x[0]`, `+0`, `(u8*)` - all emit a bare symbol store expanded through `$at`. **`park.py unpark` deletes a park's prose NOTE along with the body when they share one `/* */` block, and `park.py repark` can then swallow the NEXT unindented `}`** - it ate a typedef and a matched function's definition in round 374 (`pSlots undeclared`, caught only by `check_bin`).
