Re-read `git diff --stat` after every repark, and re-declare whatever the body's own park block carried. **A 500-instruction FRESH body can match in ONE 150-minute lane - do not pre-park it out of fear** (round 375: `field/misc5` func_800A5C40, 505 instructions / 0x7C8 bytes / 102 callees / never written before, matched at retail's exact byte count with a JTBL-only diff and `check_bin field` OK). What made it work: write the `.s` out in ADDRESS ORDER as phases (teardown, file juggling, the `switch` whose case bodies follow retail's listing order, the shared tail), get each callee's arity and argument meaning from `ghidra_info.py <Func>` instead of guessing, and diff after each phase so a wrong local shows up while the phase is small. **When a register residual will not move, prove it with cc1's allocator priorities instead of trying an eighth spelling** (round 375, battle/misc26): cc1 `-dl`'s `.lreg` dump gives `pri = floor_log2(n_refs) * n_refs / live_length` per allocno, and when two values are a few percent apart in the wrong direction (pPacket 13939 vs pRec 13406) the tie is a `global_alloc` sort that no spelling reaches - put that arithmetic in the park note and stop, because the next agent will otherwise spend a lane on it. **A park note's BYTE COUNT is the most unreliable thing in it - take retail's size from the symbol table, not from the note.** Round 377 found three parks whose recorded size was wrong: `menu/misc10` func_801E31C0 was briefed as "one instruction short, retail 1024" and retail is 1020 (the next symbol `MenuApplyDressingRoomSlotHpBonus` starts at 0x801E35BC), so the body is at retail's EXACT size and the missing instruction never existed; `battle/misc13` func_80086028's "432 bytes" was a phantom 8-byte gap that FIVE rounds chased, and it is 424; `field/misc8` func_8008492C's "280" was 276. Compute it yourself before believing a deficit: read the function's own `.size` in the `.s`, or the next symbol's address in `config/symbol_addrs.<bin>.txt` minus this one. A body that is at retail's size with a residual of a few displaced instructions is a DIFFERENT job (order/registers) from one that is short. 
- id: m-a-park-one-mask-short | keywords: park, stale, prototype, void, return, andi, mask, sll, s32
  **A PARK EXACTLY ONE MASK INSTRUCTION SHORT IS OFTEN A MISSING RETURN VALUE - check the parked body's prototype against the epilogue, not the value range.** round 389, `lib_gear/main/main` func_801E6830: four rounds of park notes argued that gcc drops the `andi` before a variable shift "because the shift count only needs its low 5 bits". The truth was that the stub was a copy of `BattleResolveTargetSlot` (twin_for strict 1.000), whose source ends `*pOut = 1 << (id & 0xFF); return id & 0xFF;` - the mask survives because the value is ALSO RETURNED, and 4 of the 7 call sites use the result (`ghidra_info.py` lists them as `[uses result]`, though its signature line said `returns value: no`). Changing the parked `void` prototype to `int` and adding the return took it 92.95 -> 100.00 with nothing else touched. So before believing a value-range wall, read the stub's epilogue: a live `$v0` that no store in the body writes is the return value, and a `void` park makes the statement that produces it unwritable.
- id: m-source-looks-like-debris | keywords: park, stale
  **Source that looks like debris can be load-bearing**: round 325 wrote the `pAngle = g_WorldMapCameraRot; pActor->state = 1;` pair TWICE, once per arm of an `if/else` on a global, and that alone moved the angle base from `$v1` to retail's `$a0` (98.65 -> 99.69). Duplicating a two-statement group per arm is legal C and it changes which register the shared base takes. Try it before blaming the allocator.
- id: m-park-note-body-rebuild | keywords: park, stale
  **A PARK WITH A NOTE AND NO BODY IS A REBUILD OPPORTUNITY, not a wall** (round 338): `field/misc6` func_8009F5F4's round-336 body had been lost (only its note survived in the file). Rebuilding it from the assembly reached 96.60 -> 98.66 at retail's exact size in one batch, because the note already carried the structure. If the comment block under the stub has prose but no C, write the C from the `.s` and keep the note's findings in mind.
- id: m-parked-note-wrong-about | keywords: park, stale, addiu, cse
  **A PARKED NOTE CAN BE WRONG ABOUT ITS OWN FIX** (round 336): menu/misc7's 99.89 note said `y - 8` inline in both calls regresses, and inlining it in both calls is exactly the match - cse then folds both uses into retail's shared register and the RTL order puts `x + 8` first (a `top` local makes gcc emit the local's addiu before the call's own). So when the note names a spelling as measured-worse, re-measure that one spelling first: the note may have been written against a different body.
- id: m-park-s-note-says | keywords: park, stale, loop, s32, u16
  WHEN A PARK'S NOTE SAYS 'NOT SOURCE-CONTROLLABLE', TREAT IT AS A HYPOTHESIS. Round 97's embedded-assignment lever broke a 99.49% park that TWO earlier workers had closed as uncontrollable, and round 89's version of a body dissolved round 77's 'D_800C3EA4 + 0xA230 wall' as soon as the shared header gained that offset. Before believing a wall, re-read the retail loop for a MISSING STATEMENT (round 93's 86.26% park was missing a whole 'unk_0x50 += 0x10000;' and had a field typed 's32' that retail compares as 'u16').
- id: m-a-permuter-find-in | keywords: park, stale, undefined, permuter, arm, branch, register
  **A PERMUTER FIND THAT DUPLICATES A BRANCH ARM MAY SCORE THROUGH AN UNINITIALISED READ - re-measure the honest spelling before trusting the park** (round 391, slus_006.64/system/archive_b func_800379D8): the parked 99.91 wrapped the error arm in `if (pFirst)` with two IDENTICAL arms, reading `pFirst` before it is ever assigned. The identical arms cost no bytes, so objdiff sees only the win in branch shape and register order, and the note recorded it as a find ('score 105 -> 85'). Rewriting it as the honest `if (nIndex >= nCount / 2) { error } else { alloc }` that retail's layout plainly is measures 99.63 in the same 432 bytes - so 99.91 was never reachable from shippable C, and the real residual is an allocator tie. Rule: when a park note credits the permuter with duplicating a branch (two arms with the same body, `if (local)` around a block that also has an else, any test on a value assigned only on the other path), check that every local the arms read is initialised on that path, then re-measure the plain if/else - the number you swap in is the number the next worker should be iterating against.
- id: m-repark-note-swallows-block-start | keywords: park, repark, lint, extern, types, include_asm
  **`park.py repark --note` appends the body's opening `/*` to the END of the note's last line, so if that line is a `//` comment the block start is swallowed and the body stays LIVE C** (round 394, field/main/misc3 func_8006FDEC): `lint_sources.py` then reports "both INCLUDE_ASM'd and defined in C" (strip_comments skips a `//` line wholesale, so it never sees the `/*`) and `diff_func.sh` scores the live body instead of the stub. Put the block start on its own line after a repark, or check `sed -n` around the stub. The same lane found the second half of this class: **a park whose externs disagree with the file's own later declarations never compiled at all** - FieldFree's block declared g_FieldDialogs/g_FieldModelFile/g_FieldWalkmesh/g_FieldScreenEffectData as `s32` where misc3.c itself declares `void*`/`FieldWalkmesh`, so cc1 exited 33 with 'conflicting types' and the recorded 98.66 was a stale number. After giving it the file's real types (`void*`, and `g_FieldWalkmesh.pFile` for the `lw $a0,%lo(g_FieldWalkmesh)` free) it re-measured the SAME 98.66 / 652 bytes, so the number stood - but two rounds had been quoting a body that could not build. Re-measure every park you touch, and read the whole first diff output (COMPILE FAILED hides the errors otherwise).
- id: m-park-note-s-exact | keywords: park, stale, frame
  A PARK NOTE'S "exact size" CAN BE WRONG, and that is why some 99.x parks never converge (round 294). objdiff's byte figure tracks YOUR object; the retail size has to be read off the original .s (or the stub's own byte count), never inherited from a note. menu/main/misc9's func_801DD5E8 was parked at 94.01% against a claimed 416 bytes when retail is 424 - the body was two instructions SHORT - and correcting the target alone moved it to 99.48 in one edit. Before iterating on a 99%+ park, count the original's instructions and multiply by 4 (or read .frame/.mask in the .s) and compare.

- id: m-park-python-drops-the-newline | keywords: park, repark, unpark, lint, swallow, include_asm
  **`park.py unpark` on the block-above-stub shape eats the newline after the body's closing brace, and the next `repark` then swallows the following function's park and its `INCLUDE_ASM` line.** Measured round 391 in battle/main/misc19: the park sat above its stub (`// notes /* body */ INCLUDE_ASM(...)`), `unpark` splices `body + text[m.end():]` where the body's own regex match ends ON the `}` (its lookahead is `\n`), so the file got `}// best 98.33%...` - the body's brace glued to the next park's note. `repark`'s DEF pattern needs that `\n` after `}`, so it did not match the body at all: it matched the `}` of the NEXT parked function, commented out that unit's note + body, and dropped the `INCLUDE_ASM(... func_8009BAC4);` line INSIDE the next park block. `lint_sources.py` caught it in one run ("the /* */ block at line 1055 holds 2 function definitions ... a park that swallowed the function after it") - so run lint immediately after any park.py call, before any diff, and if it complains, `git checkout` the file and redo the note/body edits with a plain editor edit instead of the tool. Cheap guard: print `grep -n '^}\|^\*/' <file>` around the park after the edit; a `}` followed by anything but a newline means unpark ran here.
- id: m-a-header-field-rename-stales | keywords: park, stale, header, field, compile, cc1
  **A STRUCT FIELD RENAME IN A SHARED HEADER SILENTLY INVALIDATES every park in every unit that still spells the old name - those parks stopped compiling and their percentages are stale.** Round 391, battle/main/misc19 func_8009BAC4 was parked at 99.72% on a body reading `p->unk7A`; BattleTableEntry had since named 0x7A `conditionFlags`, so the un-parked body died with `structure has no member named 'unk7A'` (cc1 exit 33, which `diff_func.sh` reports as COMPILE FAILED - read that line, not the absence of a diff). After the two-site rename it re-measured at the SAME 99.72% / 720 bytes, so the note's diagnosis survived, but nothing in the repo had compiled that body for many rounds. So after renaming a field under `include/`, grep the parks: `grep -rn '<oldName>' src/<bin>/` - a hit inside a `/* */` block is a park that no longer builds.

- id: m-unpark-glues-then-repark-swallows | keywords: park, repark, unpark, include_asm, stale, note, linker, swallowed
  **`park.py unpark` LEAVES THE BODY'S `}` GLUED TO THE NEXT FUNCTION (`}void Next(...)`) AND A FOLLOWING `park.py repark` THEN EATS THAT FUNCTION INTO THE PARK** (round 396, world_map/main/misc13). repark's body regex ends on a `}` that is followed by a newline, and the glued `}void` is not, so it keeps matching to the NEXT definition's closing brace: the reparked block then closes after WorldMapBuildDirectionFromPositions, that function is silent-dead C, and the only symptom is `undefined reference to WorldMapBuildDirectionFromPositions` at the LINK - diff_func.sh, lint_sources.py and the per-function percentage all look healthy (the stub's body was unchanged and still scored 98.21). So after any unpark+repark pair: grep the object (`nm build/src/<unit>.c.o | grep ' T '`) for the function that FOLLOWED the park, and put a newline between the unparked body's `}` and the next definition before reparking. Same shape hit again in round 404 (battling/main/misc3 func_80076884, parked 99.43): here `lint_sources.py -q` PASSED with the next matched function already inside the park, so the reliable detector is not lint but the comment census - `text.count('/*')==text.count('*/')` - plus a count of the definitions before and after (`grep -oE '^[A-Za-z_][A-Za-z0-9_* ]*\b(func_[0-9A-F]+|Battle[A-Za-z]+)\(' | sort | diff` against HEAD); the bad file there had one extra `*/` and a `BattleUpdateActorStatus` body swallowed, and rebuilding the park from `git checkout HEAD` plus three line edits was the fix.

- id: m-a-park-s-wrong-field | keywords: park, stale, register, swapped, regalloc, refs, field
  **A PARK'S "PURE REGISTER-NAMING" RESIDUAL CAN BE A WRONG FIELD IN ONE COMPARISON, because `diff_func.sh`'s diff is a TEXT diff that renames registers and cannot see that two differently-named registers hold different values** (round 397, battling/main/misc4 func_80078920, 97.75 -> 98.11 and the whole decision tree became exact, at retail's 1024 bytes / 255 insns). Retail's fourth overlap clause is `slt v0,v1,a1` with `$v1 = p->baseVertexPos.y` and `$a1 = q->baseVertexPos.y`; the park compared `p->baseVertexPos.y < q->edgeMidPos.y`, which compiles to the same two instructions with the register roles permuted (`slt v0,a0,v1`) and reads like an allocator tie in the diff. Fixing the field also equalised the REFERENCE COUNTS of all four y values (5 each), which is what the allocator needed: it then handed out `$a2/$a1/$v1/$a0` exactly as retail does. So before believing a register-only park note, decode each `lw` offset in BOTH bodies (`2352 = baseVertexPos.y`, `2368 = edgeMidPos.y` on this struct) and check that every slt's two operands are the same PAIR of fields, not just the same instruction shape; one wrong field is invisible to objdiff's fuzzy score, which moved only 0.36% for a whole clause being wrong.

- id: m-read-and-write-of-one-local | keywords: park, stale, undefined, behaviour, branch, missing, sequence-point
  **A PARK THAT READS A VARIABLE THE SAME EXPRESSION ASSIGNS (`x != (x = 0)`, `x = x++`, `f(x, x = 0)`) SCORES HIGHER BY DELETING RETAIL'S BRANCH, because cc1 performs the store first and folds the compare** (round 406, slus_006.64/system/archive func_80029EB0: the 93.58% / 940-byte park ended `if (nClose != (nClose = 0)) { return -6; }`; there is no sequence point between the read and the store, cc1 2.7.2 assigns first, the condition becomes `0 != 0`, and retail's `bnez $v1` + `li $v0,-6` pair vanishes - so the parked body returns 0 where retail returns -6 on CD retry exhaustion, and the two missing instructions are the whole reason it read 940 against retail's 944). The honest `if (nClose != 0) return -6;` measures 93.07% / 960 / 240 and its residual is a different, real one (retail narrows the HImode counter once into the register it re-tests, cc1 keeps the int and narrows twice). So before trusting any park whose body has a branch in it, grep its C for a double use of one variable in one expression: a missing branch is invisible to a percentage that is HIGHER, and the compiler is free to pick the shape that scores best. Same family as the uninitialised-local park in round 274.
  Second way in, measured round 418 (battle/main/misc11 func_8007FBE0): `park.py repark --note "PARKED 99.39% ..."` writes the note VERBATIM between the prose block and the body block, so a `--note` whose text has no comment marker is bare code between two comments. `lint_sources.py -q` passes and the only symptom is `check_bin.sh battle` dying with `[code=33] build/src/<unit>.c.i:1197: parse error before '99.39'` (ninja's message is hidden behind `>/dev/null`). Start every `--note` line with `//`, and re-run `diff_func.sh` after a repark - it reports the same cc1 error in 5 seconds.

- id: m-twin-for-misses-a-one-argument-twin | keywords: park, twin, twin_for, family, variant, envelope
  **`twin_for.py` reports "no twin" for a stub whose twin sits in the SAME unit and differs in one argument value only, because the tool compares bodies, not the surrounding family.** Round 398, slus_006.64/system/sound: func_8003D9A4 (parked 63.84%) is SoundScriptSetTriangleEnvelope0 - the matched function immediately ABOVE it - with the script's mode byte in place of the constant 3; func_8003DD24/func_8003E04C are SoundScriptSetTriangleEnvelope1/2 with the same substitution. Porting the twin's exact shape (nested `if (note) if (velocity)`, `s16 velocity` so the square gets its `sll/sra` copy, `flags = ((mode & 0x10) == 0) * 2` before the call with `flags + 1` at the store site) took func_8003D9A4 from 63.84 to 91.13% at retail's exact 268 bytes / 67 insns and func_8003DD24 from 78.85 to 84.75%. So read the two neighbours above and below a stub before trusting "no twin": a run of near-identical functions differing only in an envelope index, a constant mode or a bit is the commonest shape in the sound/menu units, and the parked body's note usually blames a register tie that the twin spelling never had.

- id: a-semantically-empty-branch-on | keywords: regalloc, la, anchor, addend, priority, branch, invariant, address
  **A SEMANTICALLY EMPTY `if (p) A; else A;` AROUND ONE CALL RAISES THAT POINTER'S ALLOCNO PRIORITY AND SO DECIDES WHICH OF TWO DERIVED ADDRESSES IS THE `la`** (round 402, menu/main/misc12 func_801E86C8, 99.79 -> 99.85 at retail's exact 688 bytes / 172 insns; not a match, but it moves the wall). Two loop-invariant bases 4 bytes apart (`pText = g_MenuSubMenuChoiceIds + 1;` with the base used inline) were materialised in the wrong direction - `lui/addiu s5 = symbol+4` + `addiu s4,s5,-4` where retail has `lui/addiu s4 = symbol` + `addiu s5,s4,4` - in retail's exact two slots. Adding a gate whose two arms are the same single call in the *first* loop after pText's last real use (`if (pText) { MenuRenderFrame(); } else { MenuRenderFrame(); }`) flips the anchor to retail's direction with the same instruction count and keeps the `la` in the `lw 852` load-delay slot; the register NAMES then remain swapped. So the la anchor is a `global.c` priority contest between the two invariant addresses, which is why the round-113 two-local spelling cannot fix it: a second *dependent* address local wins the direction but displaces the pair into the prologue save region (88.09%, re-measured with this gate). Try the empty-branch gate before permuting any body whose only residual is which of `sym` / `sym+N` is the `la`; the permuter finds nothing else here (three runs, rounds 104, 285, 402).

- id: m-a-permuter-zero-on-dead | keywords: park, permuter, zero, dead, artefact, arm, local, score
  **A `PERMUTER_ZERO` WHOSE ONLY EDITS ARE DEAD CODE REPRODUCES THE BASE BODY'S BYTES, SO PORT IT BEFORE YOU BELIEVE IT - `permute.sh` can report 0 for a candidate that cannot be a match** (round 412, battling/main/misc4 func_8007AC3C, body parked at 88.89% / 472 bytes / 118 insns against retail's 117). The run printed `PERMUTER_ZERO ... base=1440 best=0 iterations=64 seconds=12` with a candidate whose two edits were a `VECTOR *new_var` local for one block-copy source and a semantically empty `else if (new_var) { pActor = &g_BattleActor1; } else { pActor = &g_BattleActor1; }`. Porting them by hand measures 88.89 / 181 differing lines - byte for byte what the un-edited body scores - because cc1 folds both: the gate on a symbol address is known nonzero and both arms assign one value, and the local is cse'd straight back into the copy. So a ZERO that arrives in 12 seconds on a body you have measured 20+ instructions short is a scoring artefact, not a find; port the edits (30 s) rather than re-running the permuter or, worse, recording the ZERO in the park note. Two cheap sanity checks: the reported `iterations` is far below the thousands a real search takes on a 100-instruction body, and the candidate's edits add no instruction to the function you are comparing against.

- id: m-hoisting-a-park-declaration-drops-a-header-volatile | keywords: park, unpark, extern, volatile, header, check_bin, declaration, neighbour
  **HOISTING A PARK BODY'S `extern`s TO FILE SCOPE TO MAKE IT COMPILE CAN REWRITE AN ALREADY-MATCHED NEIGHBOUR, AND objdiff STILL SHOWS THAT NEIGHBOUR AT 100.00 - only `check_bin.sh` sees it** (round 428, field/main/misc2 func_80074108, parked 97.44%; hoisting the park's `extern FieldActor* g_FieldActors;` grew the matched `FieldAnyActorHasFlag8000` 100 -> 108 bytes and pushed EVERY later function in the overlay down 8, so `make check` failed on field.bin while the neighbour's own diff was untouched). `include/field/actor.h` declares that global `extern FieldActor* volatile g_FieldActors;`, so the un-park's re-declaration dropped the `volatile` and the reader's loop was optimised differently. The object diff that names it without a rebuild of anything else: `python3 -` + pyelftools comparing `.text` size and each `STT_FUNC`'s `st_size` between HEAD's `build/src/<unit>.c.o` and the new one - one `SECTION .text` line plus the +8 name is conclusive, and `culprit.sh <bin>` then points at the whole overlay. So before committing an un-park, grep every hoisted `extern` for a header declaration and keep the header's type INCLUDING qualifiers; a park's declarations belong inside its comment block unless they are known-clean, and `lint_sources.py` cannot see any of this.

- id: m-extern-declaration-form-is-a-unit-wide-score-knob | keywords: park, extern, declaration, array, decay, scalar, base, score
  **THE DECLARATION FORM OF ONE GLOBAL IN A UNIT MOVES THE SAME FUNCTION BODY BY 8 POINTS, AND A PARK MEASURED UNDER THE OTHER FORM READS AS A STALE NUMBER THAT IS ALSO UNDEFINED BEHAVIOUR** (round 438, field/main/main func_80077AB4). `extern s32 g_X[];` + `p = g_X;` (array decay) builds the symbol ADDRESS in a callee-saved register and scores 75.93% / 424 bytes / 106 insns; `extern s32 g_X;` + `p = &g_X;` scores 68.07% on the identical body (65.88% with the body this park replaced), because the scalar form lets gcc re-form the count load as `lui %lo` + `lw` instead of reaching it through the held base. Both are needed by DIFFERENT functions of the same file (func_80077884 wants the scalar form, 90.25 vs 87.24), so the file can only carry one and the other function's note must record which. Worse, the park written under the array form (`pCount = g_X;`) still compiles under the scalar form as an int-to-pointer ASSIGNMENT and re-measures 69.75% - HIGHER than the well-defined body - by dereferencing the count VALUE; that is the `a park can score high through undefined behaviour` rule with a concrete instance. Before believing any park note that names a global through a pointer local, check the extern's current declaration in the file.
## compiler dumps and probes

symptoms: need allocator dump, odd objdiff percentage, probe
try: probe dump cc1 objdiff
- id: n-cc1-s-dg-dl | keywords: probe, dump, cc1
  **cc1's `-dg`/`-dl` allocator dumps ARE available, and they are FILES, not stdout** (round 383, battle/misc26): they are written as `<input>.greg` / `<input>.lreg` NEXT TO the input, so `cc1 -dl -dg ... 2>&1 | head` prints nothing and earlier rounds wrongly concluded the dumps are unavailable in this repo. `build/src/<unit>.c.i.greg` and `.lreg` give `Register dispositions` and per-pseudo refs and live lengths directly, which turns an "allocator tie" into a number you can check. Read them before claiming a tension is unreachable.
- id: n-objdiff-s-fuzzy-percentage | keywords: probe, dump
  **objdiff's fuzzy percentage is NOT monotone in the diff count** (round 328): a body differing in 12 instructions read 98.53 where an earlier one differing in 13 read 98.62. Judge a park by counting the differing instructions (or by `check_bin`), not by the percentage, and never compare two parks' percentages as if they were a score.

- id: n-a-frame-size-change-rescores-every-spill | keywords: probe, objdiff, frame, percentage, spill, offset, compare
  **A VARIANT THAT CHANGES THE STACK FRAME RE-SCORES EVERY SPILL OFFSET IN THE FUNCTION, SO objdiff READS A COLLAPSE (75.91 to 16.59) ON A BODY THAT MOVED TOWARD RETAIL - when two candidate spellings of one function differ in frame size, compare the count of verbatim side-by-side lines, not the fuzzy percentage** (round 434, battle/main/misc23 func_800A7064, a 569-instruction 20-argument body): the park spelling with `s16` parameters was 543 instructions / 2172 bytes / frame 0x138 / objdiff 75.91, and the same body with three 32-bit parameters plus three `s16` narrowing locals (which reproduces retail's hoisted `sll 16`/`sra 16`) was 548 instructions / 2192 bytes / frame 0x120 / objdiff **16.59** - closer to retail's 569 / 2276 / 0x130 on every count that matters, yet scored 59 points lower because retail's frame is 0x130 and every `lw`/`sw` through `$sp` shifted. Measuring with `DIFF_FULL=1 tools/scripts/diff_func.sh <unit> <Func>` and counting lines that contain no `|` gave 340 for the s16 spelling and 325 for the s32 one, which is the honest ranking (the s16 spelling won on that count too). Use the byte count, the instruction count, the frame and the verbatim-line count together; a fuzzy drop of more than a few points between two variants of the same body is almost always a frame or a symbol-table difference, not a worse body.
- id: n-cc1-always-shares-a-twice-used | keywords: probe, cse, la, symbol, address, store, volatile, offset0
  **THIS cc1 ALWAYS FOLDS A TWICE-USED SYMBOL ADDRESS INTO ONE SHARED `la`, SO A RETAIL BODY THAT FORMS THE OFFSET-0 LOAD AND STORE SEPARATELY IS NOT REACHABLE FROM ANY LVALUE SPELLING - prove it with cc1_probe before spending diff runs** (round 418, field/main/misc2 func_80072D74, parked 99.06% at retail's exact 1212 bytes / 303 insns). Retail's eye-vx step is two bare symbol accesses (`lui a1,%hi(g_CameraEye)` + `lw a1,%lo(a1)` for the load, `lui at,%hi` + `sw v0,%lo(at)` for the store, i.e. maspsx's fold of two single-use la pairs) while every C spelling of the same step gets `lui t1,%hi` + `addiu t1,t1,%lo` + `lw a1,0(t1)` and stores through that one live register, which takes $t1 and shifts the step square's `mflo` to $t2. A cc1_probe of the step in isolation shows the rule directly: an address used ONCE compiles to a symbolic load (`lw $6,g_CameraEye2`), an address used TWICE compiles to one `la $8,g_CameraEye` used by both the load and the store - and that holds for the macro as written, the compound assignment `g_CameraEye.vx += (target - g_CameraEye.vx) / step`, the `*(s32*)&g_CameraEye` cast on both sides, a volatile store, and volatile on BOTH accesses (the volatile pair keeps the shared la and only adds `.set volatile`). Offsets +4 and +8 of the same vector in the same function match, so the residual is exactly the 0-offset site; cdk scores 69.05 on this body and a 1130-iteration permuter run is base=285 best=285. Read a park that blames "the load and store RTL" on an offset-0 pair as this wall and leave it.

## typing a run of halfword globals, and an empty statement as an allocation barrier

symptoms: three stores to consecutive halfwords, register allocation, load order, extra or missing nop, wrong destination register
try: struct lui gp address cast, move copy missing extra, delay-slot nop jal slot
- id: n-three-s16-globals-are | keywords: struct, lui, gp, address, cast, halfword, s16, vector, allocation
  **Three consecutive `s16` globals written from three calls are one vector field, and typing them as one struct moves the allocator** (round 389, field/main/misc7 func_800984EC): with three separate `extern s16 D_800B21AE/B0/B2` the body scored 78.46% / 196 bytes; as `extern SVECTOR D_800B21AE;` + `.vx/.vy/.vz` (offsets +0/+2/+4 of the same base) blocks 2 and 3 became instruction-identical and it read 88.37% / 204. The store target's form changes which registers the *next* statement's loads land in, so try the typed form before hunting the allocator (misc2/misc3 read the same three halfwords as one vector, which is the evidence the type is right).
- id: n-an-empty-statement-between | keywords: store, reload, order, barrier, allocation, nop
  **An empty statement (`do { } while (0);`) between two statements is an allocation barrier cc1 honours** (round 389, same function): inserted between a `sh` of a call result and the next `|=` of a byte global it stopped cc1 hoisting the following statement's `lw` above the `sh`, taking 88.37% -> 91.25% and the byte count from 204 to retail's 208. It is not shippable source (a local two-step `u8 x = g | 0x80; g = x;` reproduced 91.25% without it), but as a diagnostic it separates "wrong allocator" from "wrong statement order": when a `lw`/`lhu` of the next statement refuses to stay below a store, test with the barrier before rewriting the body.
- id: n-a-do-while-0-is-a-barrier | keywords: regalloc, do-while, barrier, callee-saved, swapped, block, tie, s1, s2
  **A `do { ... } while (0)` IS AN ALLOCATION BARRIER TOO, SO A PARKED BODY THAT WRAPS ITS HEAP PAIR IN ONE CAN BE ONE EDIT FROM 100%: delete the wrapper (a bare `{ ... }` block is enough) and a swapped callee-saved pair can fall into place with no byte change** (round 420, slus_006.64/system/archive_b func_800379D8 -> matched 100.00% at retail's EXACT 432 bytes / 108 insns, Default preset). The park sat at 99.68% with the whole instruction stream identical and only `nSecondSize` and `pSecond` mirrored between `$s1`/`$s2` (retail: second size `$s2`, pSecond `$s1`); the `-da` dump said that pair could never flip by spelling - `5 refs / 13 insns` against `5 refs / 17 insns`, so the refs/live quotient hands $s1 to the size temp in both gcc 2.7.2 AND 2.6.0 (99.68 under both) - yet a 3-minute permuter run hit PERMUTER_ZERO in 18 iterations with the single edit "drop `do { } while (0)` around the `nFirstSize`/`pFirst`/`nSecondSize`/`pSecond` group". Naming vs inlining the second size made no difference (both 99.68 with the wrapper, both 100.00 without), so the wrapper WAS the whole residual: the park notes' "do-while(0) around the two alloc pairs is load-bearing" was right about the sensitivity and wrong about the direction. Read this with n-an-empty-statement-between above: the empty-statement form of the barrier is a diagnostic, but an existing `do { } while (0)` in a body is a suspect, and removing one is free (semantically identical) - test it before spending a lane on a callee-saved quotient.

## presets, Psy-Q and tooling facts

symptoms: wrong preset or compiler, psyq stub, overlay assembler
try: preset psyq overlay
- id: o-permuter-pycparser-mangles-sizeof | keywords: permuter, pycparser, sizeof, compile, base, tooling, index
  **decomp-permuter's pycparser round-trip MANGLES `i * sizeof(T) + (int)ptr` INTO `i * (sizeof((T) (+((int) ptr))))`, so the base fails to compile with "conversion to non-scalar type requested" and every run dies before scoring** (round 409, field/main/misc3 FieldFree). The permuter re-prints the whole preprocessed file from its AST, which is why the error line (1915) does not match `permuter/<Func>/base.c` (1786); reproduce it with `ast_util.parse_c(src)` + `ast_util.to_c(ast)` in the container and compile the output to see the mangled line. `sizeof` in that sum is load-bearing for the match (it decides the `addu` operand order), so substitute the size as a literal for the run only - `i * 0x5C + (int)g_FieldActors` scored the identical 98.66 - then port the find back to the `sizeof` spelling and confirm.
- id: o-untied-hi-lo-split-is | keywords: preset, compiler, cc1, la, lui, addiu, symbol, split
  **AN UNTIED SYMBOL ADDRESS (`lui $tN,%hi(sym)` THEN `addiu $aN,$tN,%lo(sym)`) IS A COMPILER-VERSION SIGNATURE, NOT A SOURCE SHAPE: gcc-2.7.2-psx and gcc-2.6.0-psx emit ONE `la $4,sym`, which maspsx leaves alone and GNU as expands TIED (`lui $4,%hi` / `addiu $4,$4,%lo`), while gcc-2.7.2-cdk-psx emits the split** (round 408, world_map/main/misc14 func_80095CD4, parked 99.93% at retail's exact 676 bytes / 169 insns, the only residual being that pair). `cc1_probe.sh <unit> <snippet.c> [--gcc gcc-2.7.2-cdk-psx]` settles it in 10 s: for a bare extern address, `&extern.field` (symbol plus offset), an unbounded array extern, `(SVec*)(u8Ptr + 0x60)` and a store-then-take spelling, 2.7.2 and 2.6.0 print `la $4,g_X` and cdk prints `lui $2,%hi(g_X) # high` / `addiu $4,$2,%lo(g_X) # low` (PROBE line: `high=$2 low=$4`). So do not spend a lane on spellings for this residual: check which compiler can emit it at all, then score that compiler on the WHOLE body before believing the unit's preset is wrong - here cdk gives 89.1% at 644 bytes (32 SHORT) because its cc1 also splits other addresses and enables -fschedule-insns/-insns2, and -fno-schedule-insns/-insns2 gives 67.8% at 676, so the body is a mixed-TU artifact: one function whose TU was built with the splitting cc1 inside a unit whose other ten matched functions drop to 75-97 under cdk. Report it and park; a preset switch or a unit split cannot buy it.
  Round 421 re-measured the one shape `tools/scripts/probe_example.c` documents as SPLITTING
  (`ProbeStoreFirst`: store through the extern in the same block, then take its address) on
  this unit's own cc1 and it prints one `la $4,g_Probe` too - so under 2.7.2 px the split is
  unreachable in every context, not just the bare-address one, and a body parked on it has no
  source left to try. The same lane measured the sibling question: making the ONE symbol-spelled
  store in that function literal (to match the other two stores' bare-constant spelling, which
  would drop its relocation) lets cc1 hoist the address into a callee-saved register and costs
  2.4 points (97.50% at the same 676 bytes), so a mixed spelling inside one function can be
  load-bearing for the frame's register set even when the alternative looks more uniform.
- id: o-unit-whose-bodies-want | keywords: preset, psyq, typedef
  **A unit whose bodies want 2.6.0 can be SPLIT around a single 2.7.2 holdout** (round 314, `menu/misc7`): `tools/scripts/split_unit.py <unit> --to <fn> --preset Default` writes the N runs, the config ranges and the gears keys, then the head+tail go into the 2.6.0 preset and the holdout's run gets a per-file key on Default. The split itself must be byte-identical (`make check`) before any preset or C change. Two hand fixes are normally needed: `shared_decls` copies typedefs a chunk already carries (delete the prepended copy) and a typedef defined just before the following run is left out of that run (add it).
- id: o-psy-q-sdk-functions | keywords: preset, psyq
  Psy-Q SDK functions match and the shipped object is available. A sig dump of six Psy-Q library versions is in ~/xw/psyq-sigs/ (sig33/35/36/37/40/43.txt: one line per exported function, TAB-separated ARCHIVE/MODULE.OBJ <tab> NAME <tab> hexwords, 0xDEADBEEF at every relocated word). Seeding awk with the function name, e.g. awk -F'<tab>' '$2=="RotMatrixX"' ~/xw/psyq-sigs/sig40.txt, gives the ORIGINAL instruction words, so a psyq body is a 1:1 transcription - that is how three 102-instruction RotMatrix bodies and one of 164 words matched on the first try (rounds 212-213). tools/scripts/psyq_names.py over the same dumps identifies an unmatched psyq stub's SDK name and the versions that agree.
- id: o-psyq-stub-whose-s | keywords: preset, psyq, cc1
  A psyq stub whose .s has words after .size is the shipped object's 16-byte tail PAD, not stray code: converting it to C deletes the pad and shifts the whole exe. It needs a config/slus_006.64.yaml carve - close the c range at the function's end, [offset, asm] over the pad, new c range naming a new .c file, committed ALONE with make check green before any C. That is the orchestrator's job, so report PAD_BLOCKED with the pad offset and length instead of writing C. Measured non-solutions: -falign-functions=16 changes nothing, and cc1 2.7.2 rejects a function aligned attribute.
- id: o-psy-q-s-bios | keywords: preset, psyq, addiu, jr, cc1, epilogue
  Psy-Q's BIOS wrappers (_card_info, InitPAD2, ReturnFromException, ChangeClearRCnt, the libsn PC* family) are hand-written assembly with NO jr ra: the object is addiu $t2,vector / jr $t2 / addiu $t1,index and nothing else, so no C function can exist (cc1 always appends its epilogue; noreturn, a trailing for(;;) and every maspsx option were measured). Leave them stubbed and put the measurement in the file comment. The __maspsx_include_asm_hack_* + maspsx-keep trick reaches objdiff 100% by pasting retail's assembly into a .c and is NOT an acceptable match here.
- id: o-mcpu-r8000-project-s | keywords: preset, psyq, delay
  `-mcpu=r8000` is NOT this project's compiler (it breaks slus_006.64 and field at link time). Do not chase it; the delay-slot shapes are source-level.
- id: o-281-is-not-an | keywords: preset, 2.8.1, psyq, overlay, unit, allocator
  **gcc 2.8.1 is ruled out for the `battling/main/misc2` overlay unit, so the "retail's allocator reuses an argument register / hands a fresh temp a higher register than $v0" signature in the battle overlays is NOT an untested-compiler question** (round 394). The unit has three stubs in that one class - func_800732AC (retail `lw t0` where our cc1 takes `$v0` with `$v0`, `$v1` and `$a3` free), func_80073DE4 (retail keeps a multi-use sum in the dying parameter's `$a1`; ours takes `$v1`) and the 99.91% park above them ("gcc 2.7.2's local_alloc will not reuse an argument register that it charged to this block; retail's compiler did") - so 2.8.1 was installed per AGENTS.md (`decompals/old-gcc` tag 0.17, `gcc-2.8.1-psx.tar.gz`, sha256 f6f6e883...) and scored with `try_flags.sh battling/main/misc2 --all --gcc gcc-2.8.1-psx`: 3DE4 falls to 61.39% and six of the unit's fourteen already-matched functions drop to 61-96% (BattleEffectInitContext 88.56, BattleStoreSlotPositions 86.87, BattleEffectSetMatrix 72.83), so 2.8.1 is not misc2's compiler and the parks stay parked. gcc 2.6.0 is out for the unit for the same reason (`--all --gcc gcc-2.6.0-psx`: BattleEffectSetMatrix 96.67, BattleTransformEntryVertex 99.66, BattleStoreSlotPositions 99.03, BattleAccumulateGaugePoints 98.11 against 100.00 for all fourteen under Default), and cdk compile-fails the unit outright. Two consequences worth a lane: a matched function being register-INSENSITIVE (100.00 under several compilers) does not make it evidence for the unit's preset, so screen with `--all` before believing a unit is on the right one; and the whole class should be left alone rather than hand-dialled per function.
- id: o-272-vs-260-operand-order | keywords: preset, 2.6.0, addu, mfhi, operand, commuted
  **The two 99.7x residuals that no spelling could close in `menu/main/misc8` are gcc-2.7.2/2.6.0 differences, and 2.6.0 is the winner: 2.7.2 canonicalises a commutative `PLUS` to the LOWER-REGISTER-NUMBER operand first, 2.6.0 keeps the source order.** Round 391: `func_801D7F50` pinned at 99.75% under Default with TEN differing `addu` lines, all of them operand swaps (retail mixes base-first and index-first exactly as the C wrote them), scores 99.95% under `gcc-2.6.0-psx` from the same source - four of the five orders fall into place - and 100.00 (verified instruction by instruction) once the prim address gets its own `s32 primOffset = (renderContext + i * 2) * 0x28;` local (lever b-pointer-local-holding-base). `func_801D84B4` pinned at 99.73% is the same story one instruction over: 2.7.2 emits the /10000 as `mfhi $t0; sra $v0,$t0,12` (HI into a fresh temp), 2.6.0 coalesces it into the destination as retail does (`mfhi $v0; sra $v0,$v0,12`), 100.00 from the identical body. Every already-matched C body in the unit is 100.00 under both, so before parking a 99.7x body whose residual is ONLY commuted operands or an `mfhi` destination, score the unit with `tools/scripts/try_flags.sh <unit> --all --gcc gcc-2.6.0-psx` - two parks closing on one compile is the signature of a wrong preset, not of unreachable allocation.
  Round 404 closed the loop on both: the third `permute.sh` run on `func_801D7F50`'s 99.75 INLINED body (no `primOffset` local, the closest of the two spellings under 2.7.2) is `PERMUTER_NONE base=50 best=50` over 1218 iterations, so the ten commuted-`addu` lines are not reachable by any register shuffle the permuter can find either - and `func_801D84B4` is 99.73 under `-O3` and under `-G0` as well as `-O2`, so no flag but the compiler version moves the `mfhi` destination. Both parks now carry the screen (`try_flags.sh --all --gcc gcc-2.6.0-psx`: six C bodies, all 100.00) and are a straight uncomment once `menu/main/misc8` joins XenoMenu260.
  Round 418 (this entry's last confirmation) re-screened the unit with BOTH bodies live - `try_flags.sh menu/main/misc8 --all --gcc gcc-2.6.0-psx` scores all SEVEN C bodies 100.00 (MenuSetStatBarQuadColors, MenuSetStatBarTileColors, MenuMaxStatValue, MenuDrawStatTable, MenuOpenCardEvents, func_801D7F50, func_801D84B4), i.e. a unit switch and not a split - and a third permuter run on `func_801D84B4` itself (3 minutes, 859 iterations) is `PERMUTER_NONE base=20 best=20`, so the `mfhi` destination is settled for that body too, not only by the misc19 twin. `menu/main/misc8` is still on the Default preset and `menu/main/misc8_r1` was split to XenoCdk back in round 313, so the switch is one per-file path (`menu/main/misc8.`) added to XenoMenu260 - `misc8_r2` must stay on Default with it. Both parked bodies now spell the constants a 1998 programmer would (`MENU_STAT_BAR_WIDTH_MAX 6400`, `MENU_STAT_LABEL_ROW_COUNT 7`, `MENU_STAT_LABEL_SHADE 0x40` in `src/menu/main/misc8.c`), which is byte-neutral and re-verified at 100.00 under 2.6.0.
- id: o-a-loop-counter-in-a-branch | keywords: preset, 2.6.0, delay-slot, loop, increment, biv, beq, counter
  **A RETAIL LOOP COUNTER'S `addiu sN,sN,1` IN THE DELAY SLOT OF A BRANCH INSIDE THE LOOP BODY IS A COMPILER-VERSION FACT, NOT A BLOCK_ROTATION SOURCE SHAPE - 2.7.2 emits a biv increment in the LATCH block and cannot move it across the loop's `if`, 2.6.0 emits it in that slot** (round 407, menu/main/misc8_r2 func_801DB39C: 98.77% / 588 bytes under Default against retail's 584, and 100.00% / 584 bytes instruction for instruction under `gcc-2.6.0-psx` from the SAME body). The unit screen `try_flags.sh menu/main/misc8_r2 --all --gcc gcc-2.6.0-psx` is nine C functions, all 100.00, so this is a unit switch (XenoMenu260), not a park. Probed with `cc1_probe.sh` on a standalone loop of the same shape (0x80 stride into a global, an `if` around two calls): 2.7.2 puts `addu $17,$17,1` in the latch block before the `slt`, and because the body's `if` splits the block, `-fschedule-insns2` cannot move the increment up; for / while / do-while / goto / continue / ++i all give the same 588-byte body with a nop in the slot. The lever `a-four-shapes-hand-editing` calls this class BLOCK_ROTATION and says "run the permuter"; for a COUNTER (biv) increment, screen the unit under 2.6.0 first - no permuter edit changes the compiler.
  One spelling detail that belongs to `b-pointer-local-holding-base`, measured on the same body under 2.6.0: `pRows = g_Menu->pItemRows->itemNameStrings; pRow = &pRows[i];` (a typed `MenuString*` local holding the array base, assigned INSIDE the loop) emits retail's base-first `addu a2,a0,s0`, where `&g_Menu->pItemRows->itemNameStrings[i]` gives the mirrored `addu a2,s0,a0` at the same 584 bytes (99.93%). So the 2.6.0 base-local failure recorded in `a-empty-do-while-block-nudges` (a `MenuString*` local inside the loop, 96.64%) does not generalise - measure it.
  Re-measured round 421 and the SWITCH/SPLIT COST of the unit is now known: cdk is 98.77 as well, and `park_sweep.py menu/main/misc8_r2` scores the unit's only other park, func_801DA5BC, 97.17 under Default against 94.65 under 2.6.0 - so switching misc8_r2 outright costs that park 2.5 points to buy this 146-instruction function, while a split at 0x1639C (func_801DB39C's file offset, the last stub of the unit) costs nothing but one more TU.
- id: o-272-does-not-explain-a | keywords: preset, 2.6.0, cdk, commuted, addu, counterexample
  **A commuted-`addu` residual is NOT automatically the 2.6.0 lever: `battle/main/misc13` func_80087AF0 scores 99.6 under BOTH gcc-2.7.2 and gcc-2.6.0 from the same body** (round 400), with 2.7.2-cdk compile-failing the file. Its three residual sites are all the one sum `g_BattleCommandQueue + slot * 0x40 + 0x3C`, where retail keeps the loaded queue base in `$v0` and the scaled slot in `$v1` (`addu v0,v0,v1`) and this build mirrors the pair; the five later reads of the same sum in the embedded-assignment form (`(pQueueRow = g_BattleCommandQueue + slot * 0x40)[0x3C]`) already match, so the note's "load-bearing" rating for that form is right and the plain subscript at the three earlier sites is what mirrors. A 1931-iteration permuter run from that body returned base=100 best=100. Read this before re-screening a misc13-sized park under 2.6.0; the lever above needs the unit's `--all` screen to show two parks closing, not one.
- id: i-jtbl-align-3-hardcoded | keywords: switch, jtbl, align, rodata, overlay, pad, parity
  **THE PARITY THAT DECIDES A C JUMP TABLE IS THE OBJECT'S `.rodata` START, NOT THE TABLE'S OWN ADDRESS: a C table lands at `object_rodata_start + 8k`, and SUBALIGN(4) in the ld script leaves that start 4-aligned, so a 4-mod-8 table IS convertible when its unit's `.rodata` starts 4 mod 8 and is NOT when the start is 8-aligned** (round 443, world_map/misc12 func_80090A84; this replaces the round-389 claim that a 4-mod-8 table is unproducible, which was true only for 8-aligned range starts). `.align 3` before a C table is hardcoded in all three cc1s and no flag moves it (round 389: gcc-2.7.2 / 2.6.0 / 2.7.2-cdk all emit `.rdata / .align 3 / $Ln:` inline at the function, `-falign-jumps=1` is rejected by 2.7.2's cc1) - that pins the table to an 8-aligned offset INSIDE its section, and it raises the section's sh_addralign to 8, but `SUBALIGN(4)` on `.world_map` (`linker/world_map.ld:7`, and the same on battle/battling/menu) caps the linked start at 4. Proof that the start is not forced 8-aligned: `world_map/main/misc5`'s rodata range starts at file 0xD4 (parity 4) and its converted func_8007BBEC's C table sits at range+0x68 = file 0x13C (parity 4) with `check_bin.sh world_map` OK. The rule to apply before writing C: `(table_addr - object_rodata_start) % 8 == 0`, where the object start is the unit's first rodata subsegment in `config/<bin>.yaml`.
  Measurements that follow the parity rule and stay valid: `battle/misc11` (range 0x8006FE7C, parity 4) has all eight tables at 8-aligned absolute addresses, so converting any one lands it at retail's offset +4 and `check_bin.sh battle` reports 343940 against disc's 343936 - a run of converted tables absorbs the later pads, the first C table pays. `world_map/misc12` (range 0xF08, parity 0) has five tail tables at parity 4 (+0x15C/+0x18C/+0x1BC/+0x1EC/+0x214) and converting func_80090A84 (99.92%, 484 bytes, every instruction identical) pads +4: 180426 against disc's 180422, first differing word a `.text` pointer at file 0x4. Two parked bodies in that unit are otherwise exact for the same reason (func_80090C68 99.91% / 428, func_80090E14 99.90% / 416).
  Moving the object's rodata start - a yaml `[off, rodata]` carve - has an extra constraint this round measured the hard way: **the carved head may contain only tables whose pointer targets are still-asm**, because splat writes `.word .L8008E5A8` (a label only the stub's own `.s` defines, via `jlabel`) and a separate data object cannot resolve a converted function's labels. Measured on world_map/misc12 with `[0xF08, rodata]` + `[0x1064, .rodata, main/misc12]` and no C changes: `make check` fails at the world_map link with seven `undefined reference to .L8008E5A8`-style errors from `asm/world_map/data/F08.rodata.s.o` for the +0x38 table, which belongs to the already-converted `WorldMapModeInitGroupRotations`. battle/misc29's carve (round 404) worked because its head table's targets were all inside the still-asm func_800B7870. So for misc12 the remaining route is a UNIT SPLIT, not a carve: move func_80090A84/C68/E14/914D0 and everything after them into a new `main/misc12_b` with `[0x1064, .rodata, main/misc12_b]` (misc12.c.o's rodata then comes to exactly retail's 0x15C head - E190's two asm tables 0x18+0x20, E4F4's C table 0x20 at +0x38, E76C's asm 0x104 at +0x58 - so the new object's .rodata starts at 0x1064, parity 4, and the five tail tables land at internal offsets 0/0x30/0x60/0x90/0xB8, every one 8-aligned, i.e. at retail's 0x1064/0x1094/0x10C4/0x10F4/0x111C with no pad).
  Older form of this entry, kept for its measurements: `.align 3` before a C jump table is HARDCODED in all three cc1s and no flag moves it. Round 389 measured a six-word `switch` compiled by the unit's own cc1: gcc-2.7.2, gcc-2.6.0 and gcc-2.7.2-cdk all emit `.rdata / .align 3 / $Ln:` in-line at the function, and `-falign-jumps=1` is rejected by 2.7.2's cc1 ("Invalid option"). Consequence, measured on `battle/main/misc11` (range 0x8006FE7C, 4 mod 8): converting any one of its eight switch stubs lands the table at retail's offset +4 and makes the unit's `.rodata` END 8-aligned, so `check_bin.sh battle` reports `build/out/battle.bin 343940` against disc's `343936` - exactly +4 no matter WHICH table is converted or how many (a run of converted tables absorbs the later pads; the first C table pays). The test is arithmetic, not iteration: retail's last table offset vs 8. Two bodies otherwise instruction-identical to retail (battle/misc11 func_800822C4 99.93% / 576 bytes and func_80082504 99.91% / 456 bytes) are parked for this reason alone, so a future fix (a linker trick that shifts the section start, or a maspsx table-alignment option) closes them with no C changes.
- id: i-jtbl-section-start-trick-does | keywords: jtbl, align, rodata, pad, linker, split, check_bin
  **"A LINKER TRICK THAT SHIFTS THE SECTION START" DOES NOT CLOSE A 4-MOD-8 JUMP TABLE, and neither does converting the whole run** (round 391, world_map/misc12 func_80090C68 99.91% / 428 bytes and func_80090E14 99.90% / 416 bytes, both instruction-identical to retail with the `jtbl_` operand as the only residual). check_bin world_map with both converted: `180426` against disc's `180422`, first differing word a `.text` pointer in misc1's rodata (TEXT_START moved +4), and the arithmetic says one alone pays the same pad (the next table then starts 8-aligned) - the unit's rodata (0x800709F8, exactly 0x258 bytes) holds nine tables at +0, +0x18, +0x38, +0x58, +0x15C, +0x18C, +0x1BC, +0x1EC, +0x214, the last five 4 mod 8, and they tile the range with no slack, so the first converted table of that run pays a pad and nothing later shrinks back. Splitting the unit so a new `.rodata` range STARTS at 0x18C does not work either: the `.align 3` raises that object section's `sh_addralign` to 8, so ld pads the section start 0x18C up to 0x190 before the table at object offset 0. The only unexplored exit is a maspsx `.align 3`-to-`.align 2` rewrite for `.rdata` case tables - **CLOSED in round 445: maspsx has no jump-table alignment of its own to rewrite.** Its only `.align 3` is emitted for `.sbss` entries (`tools/maspsx/maspsx/__init__.py:560`, chosen by declaration size), and cc1's `.align 3` before a table is passed through untouched, so no existing preset flag or maspsx version can move a C table off an 8k object offset. Same round, `world_map/main/misc5` func_8007C3B8 shows the terminal shape of this family to recognise before spending a lane: body instruction-identical (permuter `base=0 best=0`), 876 bytes = retail's exact size, and `check_bin.sh world_map` differs in exactly 15 words, ALL inside the table range - the 4-byte pad before the table against retail's leading entry, retail's trailing zero word against our last entry, and the one `lw %lo` addend - with no word outside the table, so the overlay does not shift and the pad is a pure relocation of 4 bytes (the splat-generated `jtbl_` .s includes that trailing pad word, which is why the parked stub build is byte-exact). Round 403 named the table that misaligns the chain: misc12's nine tables tile 0x258 with no slack as E190 0x18@+0, E190 0x20@+0x18, E4F4 0x20@+0x38, **E76C 0x104@+0x58** (65 words - the only size there that is not a multiple of 8, so it is what puts every later table at 4 mod 8), then A84 0x30@+0x15C, C68@+0x18C, E14@+0x1BC, 914D0 @+0x1EC and @+0x214; a third finished body joined the family (`func_80090A84`, 99.92% / 484 bytes = retail's exact size, instruction-identical, body parked and ready to un-park) and pays the same +4. Also: both park bodies were stored in a spelling that did not compile (a scalar `g_WorldMapViewRotationY + 0x400` against the file's array extern - cc1 "invalid operands to binary &"), which is why `cc_sweep.py` shows `failed` for them under every compiler; the honest spelling is `g_WorldMapViewRotationY[0]` with a `u16` array extern, because every case body reads it with `lhu`.
- id: i-jtbl-midchain-4mod8 | keywords: jtbl, align, rodata, pad, chain, arithmetic, check_bin
  **AN 8-ALIGNED RANGE END DOES NOT SAVE A CHAIN THAT CONTAINS A 4-MOD-8 TABLE, and a single converted table in an owning unit lands at the START of the object's C `.rodata`, not at its retail offset** (round 396, world_map/main/misc5 func_8007C3B8, parked 98.13% at retail's exact 876 bytes / 219 insns for a scheduler reason of its own). misc5's rodata range is 0x8006FBC4 + 0x1C8 - an 8-ALIGNED SIZE, which is what the align-3 lever's test asks for - and its seven tables tile it at 0x0/0x30/0x50/0x68/0x8C/0x194/0x1AC, the last three 4 mod 8. Apply that test to every table, not to the last one: with all seven emitted from C the offsets become 0x0/0x30/0x50/0x68, then 0x8C pads to 0x90 (0x104 bytes, ends 0x194), the next table pads 0x194 -> 0x198 (+0x18) and the last 0x1B0 (+0x1C) ends 0x1CC - +4 over retail, so TEXT_START moves and check_bin fails (arithmetic from asm/world_map/data/main/misc5.rodata.s, not a check_bin run). Converting ONE stub measures the second half of the rule: misc5's already-matched WorldMapUpdateActorState owns the unit's first C table (0x30 bytes) and C `.rodata` precedes asm `.rodata` in the object, so the asm-owned tables follow it and a newly converted switch's table is emitted at object offset 0x30 - diff_func.sh shows `lw v0,48(at)` where retail has `lw v0,0(at)`. In an owning unit a switch stub is therefore only ever a whole-family change, and the family's FIRST table is what pays the pad.
- id: i-table-is-always-8-aligned | keywords: jtbl, align, rodata, invariant, split, carve, order
  **WRONG AS AN INVARIANT - read `i-jtbl-align-3-hardcoded` instead (round 443).** A C table is 8-aligned relative to its object's `.rodata`, not in absolute terms: the section's sh_addralign is raised to 8 by `.align 3`, but `SUBALIGN(4)` in the ld script caps the linked start at 4, and `world_map/main/misc5` (range start 0xD4, parity 4) has a C table at file 0x13C, parity 4, with `check_bin.sh world_map` OK. The wall this entry described for world_map/misc12 is real but its cause is misc12's 8-aligned range start (0xF08), not an alignment invariant. The round-404 measurements it was built on stand: func_80090C68 / func_80090E14 re-measured from their stored bodies at 99.91% / 428 bytes and 99.90% / 416 bytes, retail's exact sizes, every real instruction identical, residual only the `jtbl_` operand and the `.L` label relocs, and `check_bin.sh world_map` with both live builds 180426 bytes against disc's 180422 - exactly +4 - because misc12's tables from +0x15C on sit at 0x80070B54 / 0x80070B84 / 0x80070BB4 / 0x80070BE4 / 0x80070C0C, all 4 mod 8 while the range starts 8-aligned. The useful converse, measured on the same chain: converting the tables BEFORE the 4-mod-8 one costs nothing, because a table whose retail offset is 8-aligned and whose size chains to the next table's offset reproduces its own layout - misc12's E190 (+0, +0x18), E4F4 (+0x38) and E76C's 65-word table (+0x58, ending at +0x15C where an asm table with no `.align` follows) all convert for free - and a retail table's trailing zero word (`E190` 6 words with `sltiu 5`, `914D0` 10 words with `sltiu 9`) IS the `.align 3` pad cc1 emits, so those tables convert byte for byte.
- id: i-const-label-table-is-4-aligned-but-hoisted | keywords: jtbl, align, rodata, const, goto, order, include_asm, hoist
  **A `static const void *const` TABLE OF LABEL ADDRESSES IS THE ONLY RODATA cc1 EMITS 4-ALIGNED (`.align 2`), SO IT CAN REPRODUCE A 4-MOD-8 RETAIL JUMP TABLE - BUT cc1 HOISTS IT TO THE TOP OF THE OBJECT'S .rodata, AHEAD OF EVERY `INCLUDE_ASM` STUB'S ASM TABLE, so it only fixes the layout when ALL of the unit's tables are const tables** (round 417, world_map/main/misc12 func_80090A84 / func_80090C68, XenoWorldMap preset). The dispatch code survives the rewrite byte for byte: `nPad = (g_WorldMapPad1Held >> 12) - 1; if (nPad < 12) goto *pCases[nPad]; goto after;` followed by `case1:`-style labels, `goto mask;`/`goto after;` arms and a `mask:`/`after:` tail emits retail's `srl / addiu / sltiu / beqz / sll / lui at,%hi / addu at,at,v0 / lw v0,%lo(at) / nop / jr v0 / nop` with the SAME registers and the same 484 / 428 bytes and 99.92 / 99.91% (residual still the table operand), and each arm keeps retail's shared `j <mask>` + delay-slot `addiu`. What fails is the layout: the object's .rodata came out `0000` = A84's twelve words, `0030` = C68's, retail's `0000` = E190's asm table (`.rodata` size stayed retail's exact 0x258 and `check_bin.sh world_map` still failed, on content at file offset 0xF08). The two forms are complementary and cannot be combined for one table: an inline `switch` table lands at its own function's position (see i-jtbl-offset-follows-file-position) and an inline-looking const table always lands at +0. The reachable fixes are therefore all-or-nothing: every table of the unit as a const label table in address order (all nine of misc12's sizes are multiples of 4, so `.align 2` reproduces retail's packed +0x00/0x18/0x38/0x58/0x15C/0x18C/0x1BC/0x1EC/0x214 exactly), or a yaml carve of the sub-range from the first 4-mod-8 table into a unit whose .rodata holds only const tables (a 4-mod-8 section start is legal for `sh_addralign` 4). Both need every stub that owns a table in the range converted first, so check the unit's other parks before starting.
  Round 430 closed the last loophole in that sentence with a probe, so do not re-open it: a
  FUNCTION-scope `static const void *const pCases[]` hoists to the object's `.rodata` top exactly
  like the file-scope one (`tools/scripts/cc1_probe.sh world_map/main/misc12` prints `.rdata`,
  `.align 2`, `t.2:` ahead of BOTH test functions' bodies), so the const-table route cannot buy
  order from inside the function either. And no carve can host a 4-mod-8 C table: cc1's
  `.align 3` pads the table to an 8-aligned offset inside its section AND raises that section's
  `sh_addralign` to 8, so ld aligns the whole section too and the table is 8-aligned in absolute
  terms whatever the yaml says - which is why misc12's five 4-mod-8 tables (0x80070B54 onward)
  stay asm even though each one is instruction-identical from C (`permute.sh` reports
  PERMUTER_ZERO base=0 in one iteration, the residual being the table's relocation).


- id: i-rodata-head-carve-moves | keywords: jtbl, carve, rodata, align, head, check_bin, chain, split
  **A 4-MOD-8 JUMP TABLE IS CONVERTIBLE AFTER ALL: CARVE THE UNIT'S RANGE HEAD OUT AS A SPLAT `rodata` SUBSEGMENT ENDING AT THE TABLE'S OWN (8-ALIGNED) ADDRESS, SO THE UNIT'S C `.rodata` STARTS THERE** (round 404, battle/main/misc29 func_800B8098, 73 insns, XenoBattleTailCdk0). misc29's range is 0xF0C..0xF38 and holds two tables: func_800B7870's 5-word `jtbl_800709FC` at +0 (still an `INCLUDE_ASM` stub) and func_800B8098's 6-word `jtbl_80070A10` at +0x14. Converting 8098 alone measured 99.86% / 292 bytes = retail's exact size with only the `jtbl_` vs `.rodata` operand left - the ordinary MATCH-JTBL shape - and `check_bin.sh battle` then failed by exactly +4 (`build/out/battle.bin 343940` vs disc `343936`) with the pad visible at 0xF20 (`0000 0000`, then the C table at 0xF24): the stub's asm table is emitted FIRST (stream order, the include sits above the C definition), then cc1's `.align 3` pads. FIX, two lines of yaml and no C change: `- [0xF0C, rodata]` + `- [0xF20, .rodata, main/misc29]`; splat then writes `asm/battle/data/F0C.rodata.s` (the same words, `dlabel jtbl_800709FC`, its own `.align 3` at object offset 0 is a no-op), `linker/battle.ld` lists it before `misc29.c.o(.rodata)`, and because 0xF20 = 0x80070A10 is 8-aligned the C table lands at retail's offset with no pad: `check_bin.sh battle` prints OK with the C body live (MISMATCH before the carve, OK after - measured both ways). The stub's `%hi(jtbl_800709FC)` still resolves, so a carve does NOT require converting the table's owner. The test is arithmetic and strictly stronger than i-jtbl-align-3-hardcoded: a table is convertible iff its ABSOLUTE address is 8-aligned and every byte before it in the unit's range is either a C table emitted at an earlier file position (i-jtbl-offset-follows-file-position) or carved; this is how the older "the range starts 4 mod 8 so this unit can never host a switch" notes should be re-read (it does not help world_map/misc12, whose offending tables are at absolute 4-mod-8 addresses).
 Round 406 measured the opposite case in `world_map/main/misc8`: its range starts 4 mod 8
(0x8007047C) and holds four tables, ALL of them 8-aligned in absolute terms (0x8007047C is
the only 4-mod-8 one, and it is a C table whose `.align 3` at object offset 0 is a no-op),
so `func_80084068` (233 insns) reaches 99.96% / 932 bytes = retail's exact size, every
instruction identical, and `check_bin.sh world_map` fails by exactly +4 (180426 vs 180422)
for the last table alone (jtbl_800704C8, absolute 0x800704C8, 8-aligned). The carve here
CANNOT be done alone: the carve point 0x9D8 sits after a C-emitted table (0x98C, from
`WorldMapUpdateSpriteSetPrims` in the same file) and after the two tables the
`func_80083A00` stub's asm owns (0x9A0, 0x9B8), so the object that emits this table must be
the one whose `.rodata` starts at 0x9D8 - i.e. the unit needs a code+rodata range split at
0x14578 into `misc8_b` (an ordinary AGENTS unit split, not a yaml-only carve). Until then
the body stays parked at 99.96%.
- id: i-unit-split-parity-4-measured | keywords: jtbl, align, rodata, split, parity, check_bin, make-check
  **`g-parity-4-split-is-the-route-for-a-4-mod-8-tail-table` IS NOW MEASURED, END TO END: the misc11 tail split closed func_8008D678 (445 insns / 1780 bytes) with `make check` all eleven OK, then `check_bin.sh world_map` OK** (round 445). The ranges are that lever's recipe exactly - `[0xA28, .rodata, main/misc11]` + `[0xDE4, .rodata, main/misc11_b]`, `[0x1AC3C, c, main/misc11]` + `[0x1DB88, c, main/misc11_b]` - and the order that makes it cheap is: split with NO C change and gate with `make check` (must stay all OK), then un-park, then `diff_func.sh` (99.96%, 1780 bytes = retail's, residual only the `jtbl_`/.rodata operand and the `.L` relocs) and `check_bin.sh` (OK). Two things to know: a rodata-only carve is NOT enough here (the head would have to emit tables whose `.L` targets belong to a converted function - the round-443 failure), and the new file needs its `gears.toml` key inside the SAME preset list as `misc11.`, which is safe because the trailing `.` in a per-file key does not match `misc11_b.c`. This also settles the round-430 sentence below in `i-jtbl-align-3-hardcoded` ("no carve can host a 4-mod-8 C table"): with a `SUBALIGN(4)`-capped overlay section a parity-4 range start hosts parity-4 tables, measured.
- id: i-permuter-zero-on-a-switch | keywords: jtbl, permuter, reloc, addend, align, rodata, placement, park
  **`permute.sh` PRINTING `PERMUTER_ZERO base=0` IN ONE ITERATION ON A `switch` MEANS THE RESIDUAL IS THE TABLE'S RELOCATION, NOT THE C - park it, and re-read any old "needs a maspsx `.align 3` rewrite" verdict against the absolute-address test** (round 414, world_map/main/misc6 func_80080370, live at 99.92% / 520 bytes = retail's exact size, 0.5 s, 1 iteration). `diff_func.sh` prints retail's `lw v0,0(at)` + `%lo(jtbl_800701E0)` against the body's `lw v0,24(at)` + `%lo(.rodata)`; the `+0x18` lives in the RELOCATION ADDEND, so the permuter's instruction-stream score is already 0 while objdiff stays at 99.92 - no C edit can move a jump table, so read base=0 as "body finished" and do not spend a second permuter run. Where such a park sits, the convertible-for-real test is `i-rodata-head-carve-moves`'s, and it needs NO maspsx change: misc6's table is at absolute 0x800701E0, which IS 8-aligned, so the yaml carve/unit-split route applies and the unit's older "maspsx rewrite" note was superseded. What blocks misc6 specifically is the one *preceding* table: the range 0x29C..0x7F4 holds eight tables at offsets 0, 0x108, 0x168, 0x1D0, 0x230, 0x338, 0x440 (all 8-aligned) and 0x454 (4 mod 8), and the 0x440 one is `jtbl_800701CC` - 5 words, 0x14 bytes, not a multiple of 8 - emitted from C by the ALREADY-MATCHED `WorldMapUpdateSlotActor`, so no carve point before 0x454 can be placed without moving that table. Two C tables whose retail addresses differ by a non-multiple of 8 can never both be `switch` tables of one section, so the fix is a unit split at the offender's file offset (0x10880 -> `main/misc6_b`, rodata range starting 0x6F0, plus a `gears.toml` path entry when the preset is keyed per file), where the table sits at object offset 0.

- id: o-260-reduces-a-param-based-index-biv | keywords: preset, 2.6.0, 2.7.2, giv, strength-reduce, biv, index, preheader
  **gcc 2.6.0 STRENGTH-REDUCES AN INDEX OF THE FORM `<parameter> * K + i` INTO A REDUCED BIV (`sll sN,param,K` IN THE PREHEADER, `addiu sN,sN,1` IN THE LATCH) WHERE 2.7.2 RECOMPUTES IT AT EVERY USE SITE - so a loop body that is exactly TWO INSTRUCTIONS / 8 BYTES SHORT, with retail holding `sll v0,s6,1 / addu v1,v0,s2` at the loop head and `sll s1,s6,1 / addu s1,s1,s2` after the calls, is a compiler difference and no spelling reaches it** (round 442, menu/main/misc8_r2 func_801DA5BC, re-parked 94.84% under XenoMenu260 at 996 bytes / 249 insns against retail 1004 / 251). Retail's loop (`for (i = 0; i < 0x10; i++)`, index `arg0 * 2 + i`) is 2.7.2 code sitting in a 2.6.0 unit: the same typed body scores 97.37 under gcc-2.7.2 and 92.96 under 2.7.2-cdk, and each already-matched neighbour of the unit needs 2.6.0. Read the preheader for the giveaway - a `sll sN,<param>,K` before the loop and a `+1` in the latch (retail has neither, it increments only the `i * 0x80` row offsets) - then stop: the register names, the prologue save order and the row-pointer allocation all cascade from that one choice, and a 1447-iteration permuter run on the 2.7.2 body found nothing. The same body is also a reminder that a `(MenuString*)g_Menu->pItemRows)[i]` cast is worth 0.19% and 10 diff lines against the typed `g_Menu->pItemRows->itemNameStrings[i]` subscript, so type the row before despairing.

## unit preset, splits and stale parks

symptoms: mfhi destination, unit measures exact under another gcc, park percentage impossible to reproduce
try: preset, split, stale, mfhi, 2.6.0

- id: p-mixed-unit-wants-a-split | keywords: preset, split, mfhi, mixed, unit, 2.6.0, 2.7.2
  **A UNIT CAN BE GENUINELY MIXED: SCREEN EVERY MATCHED FUNCTION BEFORE BELIEVING A PARK'S PRESET LEAD.** `battle/main/misc19`'s `func_8009BAC4` is byte-exact under gcc-2.6.0 (all 180 instructions and 11 table words, residual only `jtbl_` vs `.rodata+0x78`, i.e. the linked-bytes-identical case) while its unit preset 2.7.2 leaves the four `mfhi` destinations differing - the documented `smulsi3_highpart` md difference - so it cannot ship in place. The whole-unit screen (`try_flags.sh battle/main/misc19 --all --gcc gcc-2.6.0-psx`) says the unit is not a 2.6.0 unit either: six already-matched functions drop (`BattleClearSlotFlag15A1` 95.71, `BattleFillPartySlotWeaponRecord` 96.15, `BattleInitItemInventoryAndConditionMasks` 99.38, `BattleSetSlotEntryFieldsTo1` 99.71, `BattleSetSlotEntryFieldsTo2000` 99.77, `BattleSetSlotFlag15A1` 99.57). So this one needs a split, not a preset switch.
- id: p-misc19-split-candidates-measured | keywords: misc19, split, 2.6.0, cdk, mfhi, carve, unit
  **`battle/main/misc19`'s split, sized in round 393.** Whole-unit screens with both stubs un-parked: under `gcc-2.6.0-psx` seven matched bodies plus `func_8009A9D0` stay 100.00 while 13 others land 95.71-99.96, so it is not a unit switch; under `gcc-2.7.2-cdk-psx` (still -G8) nine matched bodies collapse to 0.00-93.17, so cdk is ruled out for this unit entirely (its jal-slot family is not here). The two stubs this lane owned are NOT one run: `func_8009A9D0` (0x8009A9D0, 116 bytes, 100.00 under 2.6.0) is bounded by `BattleFillPartySlotWeaponRecord` (96.15 under 2.6.0) and `BattleSetSlotFlag15A1` (99.57), i.e. a one-function carve-out; `func_8009A2D4` (0x8009A2D4, 1252 bytes) scores only 99.52 under 2.6.0 with its best body, so carving it buys nothing - its 99.19%/1256 park is also 4 bytes OVER retail (the `move a2,s2` of the second pCondition pointer), which is the combination its own note says the body cannot reach: reload + no copy is retail's 1252 at 98.21, reload + copy is 1256 at 99.19, and the mfhi/mflo destinations cap it below 100 either way.
  Round 439 EXECUTED the first of those carve-outs and it closed: `config/battle.yaml` now tiles `main/misc19` as three c ranges - `[0x2A5EC, c, main/misc19]` (head), `[0x2B2B0, c, main/misc19_r2]` (only `func_8009ADA0`) and `[0x2B40C, c, main/misc19_b]` (the tail, Default) - with `battle/main/misc19_r2.` on XenoLibrary and `battle/main/misc19_b.` on Default's per-file `paths`. `func_8009ADA0` is 100.00% (348 bytes / 87 insns = retail's) on the FIRST diff from its parked body, and it stays 100.00 with the body rewritten in the typed form (`BattleTableEntry* p = &g_BattleCurWorkBlock->slots[arg0];` plus `p->maxHp / 20`, `p->maxMp / 20`, `p->state.maxFuel / 50`, `p->activeEffectFlags1`), so the four divisions cost nothing once the compiler is right. Three layout facts that a `c`-range carve of a unit which owns asm rodata must also satisfy: (1) the unit's `.rodata` range has to travel with the functions that own the tables - `[0x938, .rodata, main/misc19]` became `main/misc19_b`, because both tables in it (`jtbl_80070428` at +0x0 and `jtbl_80070478` at +0x50) belong to `BattleDecrementGearPartUse` and `BattleSetCommandCodeForSlotKindFlag`, which are both in the tail; (2) every new TU needs its own header block, and the externs the old head carried for other units are dead weight (only `try_flags`-style screens notice) - build each file's externs from what its own functions use; (3) `make check` with NO C change is the gate for the carve alone (all eleven OK here, both before and after un-parking), and `check_bin.sh battle` afterwards. The remaining carve candidate in this unit is `func_8009A9D0` (0x2AEE0..0x2AF58, 100.00 under 2.6.0 / 99.66 under Default): it now needs `main/misc19` closed at 0x2AEE0, a `main/misc19_r3` for it on XenoLibrary and a third Default file for 0x2AF58..0x2B2B0.
- id: p-split-unit-corrupts-misc19 | keywords: split, tool, duplicate, redefinition, parked, prototype
  **`tools/scripts/split_unit.py` DUPLICATES C DEFINITIONS ON A UNIT WITH PARKED BODIES.** `split_unit.py battle/main/misc19 --to func_8009BAC4,... --preset XenoLibrary` produced a `misc19.c` whose own `.i` redefines `BattleGetActorConditionCode` and `FieldScriptClearAnimationOverrideIfFlag16` (ninja: `redefinition of ...`, exit 33); `--dry-run` looked correct (2 runs, 1 new file), so check the generated head file for duplicated chunks before `make check`. Reverted cleanly with `git checkout -- config/battle.yaml gears.toml src/<unit>.c && rm src/<unit>_r1.c`. Use it only after reading where its chunk markers land in a file that has `INCLUDE_ASM` lines *and* live C for the same function.
- id: m-stale-park-scores-from-an-old-object | keywords: stale, park, percent, compile, cc1, renamed, header
  **A PARK'S PERCENTAGE CAN COME FROM AN OBJECT BUILT BEFORE A HEADER RENAME.** Two bodies in round 391 did not compile at all (`structure has no member named 'unk7A'` after `BattleTableEntry` had already named it `conditionFlags`; `invalid operands to binary &` on an array extern) yet carried 99.72 / 99.91 notes: `diff_func.sh` prints nothing and exits 33 on a compile failure, so a stale `.o` gets scored. Un-park and re-measure FIRST, and treat a note whose percentage looks better than its own body can produce as unmeasured.
- id: p-gprel-tail-means-the-unit-boundary-is-wrong | keywords: gp_rel, gp-relative, split, boundary, common, tentative, extern, tail
  **A RETAIL TAIL THAT STORES A GLOBAL AS `sb $v0,%gp_rel(sym)($gp)` WHILE YOUR `extern` DECLARATION COMPILES TO `lui $at,%hi` + `sb %lo($at)` (TWO insns) MEANS THE OBJECT THAT OWNED THAT FUNCTION *DEFINED* THE SYMBOL AS SMALL DATA - and if an already-matched function in the same unit reaches the same symbol ABSOLUTELY (`lui %hi` + `lbu`), the two cannot be one TU and the retail object boundary (i.e. the `config/<bin>.yaml` c-range split) is wrong, not the C** (round 445, `slus_006.64/system/temp3` func_8001B970, parked 86.53% / 404 bytes / 101 insns against retail's 424 / 103). Evidence measured on the same body in two runs: with `u8 g_GameResumeScene; u8 g_MenuSavedMainMenuChoice;` as tentative definitions in temp3.c (which already does exactly that for `g_DebugPageIndex` at the end of the unit, and that one links in matching mode) the tail becomes retail-exact and the body reads **88.67%, 396 bytes, 99 insns**; but `GameSetUpDisplayAndChangeState`, matched at 100.00% in the same file, drops to **97.71%, 376 bytes** because its two `g_GameResumeScene` reads turn into `lbu 0($gp)` where retail has `lui %hi`/`lbu`. So the fix is a carve: a TU starting at func_8001B970 (file 0xC170, not the 0xC350 the yaml uses) that defines the two globals, with `GameSetUpDisplayAndChangeState` left in `system/temp3`. Two things to check before trusting a park on a function like this: `grep gp_rel` the stub's `.s` for its tail (that is a 5-second read that names the blocker) and remember `-fcommon` is in the cc1 flags, so a tentative definition is a common that the linker resolves to the retail address rather than a definition that moves .bss.
- id: p-own-rodata-ok-does-not-mean-the-range-is-one-object | keywords: rodata, carve, own_rodata, jtbl, strings, split, unit boundary
  **`own_rodata.py`'s OK MEANS "NO FOREIGN LABEL IN THE RANGE", NOT "ONE OBJECT": a carved `.rodata` range that starts with a jump table and then holds string literals is TWO objects, because cc1 emits named `.rodata` (strings) before the tables it generates for `switch`es, so retail's order `[table][strings]` cannot come out of one TU whose only .rodata is those two (round 450, `slus_006.64/system/controller_c`: range 80018B58..80018BA4 = a 12-byte table then `g_StrDebugRobotFormat/ShopFormat/CdFormat`, which `system/menu.c`'s matched `MenuExecute` reads). Carving it fails the link with `undefined reference to g_StrDebugRobotFormat` because the asm blob no longer defines the strings, and defining them in the unit puts them BEFORE the table. The table's owner object ends where the strings begin, so such a range needs its own cut at that boundary, or it stays `jtbl-not-owned`.
  Same test for the other two refusals: `menu/main/misc1` (`g_MenuGameSerialCodeDiscOne`/`DiscTwo`) and `movie/main/misc1` (`D_8006FD88`, `D_8006FDB0`, `D_8006FDBC`, `D_8006FDE0`).
- id: p-dont-expand-li-and-cdk | keywords: preset, delay-slot, la, li, addiu, ori, cdk, maspsx
  **TWO ASSEMBLER-LEVEL FACTS CLOSE A `la`/`li` PARK THAT NO SPELLING CAN REACH: maspsx `--dont-expand-li` (retail's assembler expands a small `li` as `addiu $r,$zero,K`, maspsx's expand_li gives `ori`), and gcc-2.7.2-cdk's SPLIT `%hi`/`%lo`, which is the only way a `lui $v0,%hi` fills a `beqz` delay slot followed by `addiu a1,v0,%lo` with a DIFFERENT destination** (round 397, `slus_006.64/system/work_list` func_8001D2B0, 81 insns). Plain 2.7.2 ties `%hi` to its destination (`lui a0` / `addiu a0,a0`) and reorg has nothing to put in the slot, so that body is one instruction long at 97.53% under the unit's Default preset and 98.27% with `--dont-expand-li` alone; cdk alone reaches 99.26% (the address pair is retail's, only `li 1` still expands to `ori`); `try_flags.sh <unit> <Func> --gcc gcc-2.7.2-cdk-psx --maspsx "--dont-expand-li --use-comm-section --run-assembler"` scores it **100.00**, and an objdump side-by-side of the whole function is instruction-identical to retail. Screen the unit with `--all` under the same combo before proposing it: here all 26 matched bodies stayed 100.00, but one of them (SpriteAnimFrameListFlush) only did so after its `curSpriteFrame` read became a `u16 animFrame` local - cdk does not CSE that reload the way 2.7.2 does, and a hoisted local restores both presets. Presets are per file, so a spot like this is a `PRESET_CDK` report (pct under cdk + --dont-expand-li, pct under unit flags, and every matched function's score), never a gears.toml edit from a worker lane.
  Round 426 re-verified the same combo from the unit's parked file with the tool itself - `try_flags.sh slus_006.64/system/work_list --all --gcc gcc-2.7.2-cdk-psx --maspsx "--dont-expand-li --use-comm-section --run-assembler"` scores 27 of 27 functions 100.00 including func_8001D2B0, i.e. no function needs the u16 fix any more - and measured what the switch does to the unit's other two parks (both live, same combo): WorkListAddTask 89.34 -> 84.03 and TimerWorkListAddTask 84.20 -> 89.55, so the switch costs one park 5 points and buys the other 5, and closes func_8001D2B0.

- id: g-array-decay-preheader | keywords: loop, preheader, addiu, lui, table, guard, reload
  **A guard that loads the bound through a materialised symbol register while the loop reloads it through a derived table base wants the unbounded-array decay plus a runtime subtract (round 399, field/scripts/virtual_machine func_800A24C4).** Retail: `lui/addiu v1,&count; lw v0,0(v1); blez; addiu s2,v1,-0x80` and the loop reloads `lw v0,0x80(s2)`. The spelling that reproduces all four: `extern s32 g_FieldActorModelSlotCount[]; pCount = g_FieldActorModelSlotCount; pAnimTable = (s16*)pCount - 0x40; for (i = 0; i < ((s32*)((u8*)pAnimTable + 0x80))[0]; i++)` - the decay materialises `&count` once (guard loads 0(reg), table derives from the same reg), the pointer VARIABLE keeps the subtract a runtime `addiu` so gcc never folds it to `la count-0x80`, and the table-spelled bound makes the reload `128(s2)`. Any constant-expression spelling of the table folds and loses the fourth instruction; spelling the bound `pCount[0]` keeps `&count` live across the loop in a callee-saved register instead.
- id: g-volatile-load-call-boundary | keywords: store, volatile, hoist, scheduler, inline, order
  **A volatile read hoisted above the next statement's loads can be pinned by routing it through a static inline helper (round 399, field/scripts/virtual_machine func_800A24C4).** gcc 2.7.2 -O2 (scheduler on) lifts a plain `*(volatile s32*)(pShake - 0x12)` out of statement order and the per-field load-delay nops vanish (76.59%); `static inline s32 F(volatile u_char* p) { return *(volatile s32*)(p - 0x12); }` called as `t[1] += F(pShake)` restores retail's in-order stream (83.68%, permuter find). The same effect asymmetrically: a `do {} while (0);` around a following statement and a `t[new_var]` index variable (new_var = 1) each block a scheduling edge; both were needed for size-exact 85.58%. Beware the permuter will also propose the fourth wrap, which scores better internally but grows the body 12 bytes - size is the gate.
