- id: o-psyq-libetc-sibling-park-is-the-shape | keywords: psyq, libetc, twin, park, sibling, handler
  **A psyq `libetc` stub is often a SHAPE copy of a parked or matched function in a sibling psyq unit, and `twin_for.py` calls it "no twin" because the bodies are not byte-identical - read the neighbour first** (round 401, `psyq/libetc/intr_dma`: `trapIntrDMA` 97 insns and `setIntrDMA` 42 insns both reached 100.00% / objdiff 100.00% on the FIRST diff, from the allocator shape visible in `intr.c`'s parked `trapIntr`: `while (mask) { for (i = 0; mask && i < N; ++i, mask >>= 1) { if (mask & 1) { ack; if (handlers[i]) handlers[i](); } } mask = reread; }`). The parked neighbour also shows the house idiom this file needed for the format strings (`extern char D_80019440; ... printf(&D_80019440, ...)` - an implicit `printf` declaration, no stdio header in `include/`), and its `for (...; mask && i < N; ++i, mask >>= 1)` spelling is what makes cc1 put the `i++` in the latch's delay slot instead of the mask shift.
- id: o-a-differing-lui-immediate-is | keywords: lui, immediate, hi, constant, width, objdiff
  **A diff whose only differing lines are `lui` immediates is a WRONG LITERAL, not an allocator tie: `objdiff` prints `lui v1,0x80` against your `lui v1,0x8000` and the percentage reads 99.95%, but the original `.s` says `lui %hi(0x800000)` (round 401, `psyq/libetc/intr_dma` setIntrDMA, 42/42 instructions, 4 differing lines).** The DICR word here is OR'd with `0x800000` (bit 23), not `0x80000000`, in BOTH arms; transcribing the disassembly's `(0x800000 >> 16)` as a 32-bit constant cost the match. Read the parenthesis in the `lui`'s operand before touching the C's shape.
- id: h-address-taken-field-pointer-forces-in-place-rmw | keywords: store, order, hoist, load, address, pointer, local, plus-equals
  **`X += 8` ON A 16-BIT FIELD OF A GLOBAL-POINTER OBJECT IS SCHEDULED OUT OF ORDER, BUT TAKING THE FIELD'S ADDRESS INTO A POINTER LOCAL (ASSIGNED AT THE STORE SITE) MAKES cc1 EMIT THE RMW STRICTLY IN PLACE** (round 402, field/main/misc7 func_800984EC, 88.37 -> MATCH at retail's exact 208 bytes / 52 insns; objdiff 99.62% only because the vy/vz stores relocate to `D_800B21AE+2` where the retail object names `D_800B21B0`/`D_800B21B2`, so `check_bin.sh field` is the gate). Retail's tail is `lbu g_FieldScreenShakeMode / lui+lw g_FieldScriptVMCurActor / ori / sb / lhu 0xCC(v1) / nop / addiu / sh` - every load after the store that precedes it; the plain `g_FieldScriptVMCurActor->scriptInstructionPointer += 8;` hoists the actor load above the preceding `sb` and puts the `lhu` in the store's delay slot (51 insns, 204 bytes, ip value `$v1`+pointer `$a0` instead of retail's `$v0`/`$v1`). An empty `do { } while (0)` after the first store moves only the pointer load into retail's slot (52 insns, 91.25%) and leaves the `lhu` hoisted; a second barrier after the `sb` gives retail's tail order but reloads the pointer (54 insns, 90.77%); a 4-minute permuter run (2696 iterations, base 295) found nothing. `u_short* pIp; ... pIp = &g_FieldScriptVMCurActor->scriptInstructionPointer; *pIp += 8;` reproduces retail's whole tail. The inline `*(&x->field) += 8;` is folded back to the plain form by cc1, so the named, uninitialised local is required - the same shape as `h-address-taken-load-blocks-hoist`, applied to a single read-modify-write instead of a group of loads.

- id: gcc-mints-a-derived-giv-for | keywords: frame, callee-saved, s0, s1, prologue, spill, giv, loop, constant offset, immediate, pressure
  **A SPILLED-INTO-s-REGISTER LOOP VARIABLE CAN COME FROM gcc's LOOP OPTIMIZER MINTING EXTRA DERIVED GIVS, not from the source's live set.** Round 409, slus_006.64/system/system func_80034FFC (424 insns, parked 58.11%): the body is structurally right and the prologue is instruction-identical, but where retail has NO frame and no callee-saved register (pGlyph = a0, pDest = a2, byte stride = t4, pAhead = t1, pBehind = t2, one derived giv t3 = pDest+2, i = t5), the same C costs `subu sp,sp,8` + `sw s0/sw s1` because cc1 replaced the `pAhead[k]`/`pBehind[k]` accesses at +2/+4 with their own induction variables (five `addu X,X,t4` per iteration) and pushed pGlyph/i into s0/s1. The check: count the `addu <ptr>,<ptr>,<stride>` at the bottom of retail's loop (three, plus pDest in the branch delay slot) against yours (five or more) before blaming the allocator; a `+4` access retail spells `lhu v0,4(t1)` is an immediate off the base, so the source reached it as a subscript of the same pointer, not through a second pointer. Measured non-fixes: declaring the third-word pointer (`pMid = pDest + 2`, used as `-1`/`0`) scores WORSE (56.32%, 427 insns) - it adds a giv instead of removing one.
symptoms: frame size off, extra stack slot, prologue save order
try: frame stack prologue spill giv callee-saved
- id: i-duplicated-arm-tails-do-not-cross-jump | keywords: switch, case, tail, merge, cross-jump, duplicate, goto, shared
  **THIS cc1 (2.7.2-psx, Default -O2) DOES NOT MERGE IDENTICAL SWITCH-ARM TAILS, so a retail block shared by three case arms has to be ONE copy in the source, reached with a `goto`** (round 407, field/main/misc8 func_80083288, 451-insn function, parked 74.01%). Retail's `flags12C_0` switch has three arms that each set a rotation component and then all jump to one `.L800833D4` block holding `RotMatrix`/`MulMatrix2`/the three translation copies before falling into a shared `CompMatrix`; scripting the tail inside all three arms (the literal reading of "each arm ends with the CompMatrix") measures 66.81% / 502 insns - three `jal RotMatrix`, no merge. Hoisting the tail below the switch and giving the three arms `goto rotate;` (the `default:` arm, case 0, keeps its own body and `break`s) gives 446 insns / 1784 bytes against retail's 451 / 1804 and all three arms sharing one tail. When a park's only size excess is a multiple of a tail's length, count the `jal`s of the shared callee in the listing before trying any other spelling.

symptoms: lui/addiu vs gp, %hi/%lo vs %gp_rel, a global the neighbours read through gp, one function in a unit with absolute addressing
try: gp gp_rel hi lo maspsx comm split tu absolute
- id: s-maspsx-gp-rel-is-per-symbol-per-tu | keywords: gp, gp_rel, hi, lo, maspsx, comm, sbss, split, tu, absolute
  **A TIED-OFFSET GLOBAL READS ABSOLUTE (`lui %hi` + `lw %lo` in the SAME register, `lui $at` for a store) EXACTLY WHEN THE SYMBOL IS NOT IN THE COMPILING TU'S SMALL DATA, AND IN A UNIT THAT TENTATIVELY DEFINES IT NO SPELLING CAN PRODUCE THAT - IT IS A SPLIT, NOT A BODY PROBLEM.** Retail `slus_006.64/system/temp2` func_80026BA4 (round 412, 138 insns, parked 68.00%) reaches `g_GfxCurWorkBuffer`/`g_GfxCurWorkBufferEnd` with the assembler's absolute macro expansion while its four matched neighbours (`GfxQueueSpriteTileData`, `GfxQueueImageTransfer`, `GfxSetRenderContext`, `SpriteDrawTile`) use `%gp_rel` for the same two symbols. gcc emits the bare `lw $8,g_GfxCurWorkBuffer` macro in BOTH cases (grep `build/src/<unit>.c.s`) and maspsx picks: `tools/maspsx/maspsx/__init__.py` rewrites to `%gp_rel` only when `symbol in self.sbss_entries`, which `preprocess_lines` fills from every `.comm sym,size` with `size <= sdata_limit` (the -G). `include/system/animation.h` declares both globals as tentative definitions, so every TU that includes it emits `.comm g_GfxCurWorkBuffer,4` and gets gp everywhere. Probes: `try_flags.sh <unit> <Func> --gp -G0` (no sbss entry, size 4 > 0) DOES produce retail's absolute `lui`/`lw` shape out of the same unit - verified by objdumping the object - so the addressing is reachable and the fix is a `config/<bin>.yaml` split at the function's file offset into a unit that only declares the externs. Screening is cheap and rules out presets on the way: unit flags 68.00, cdk -G0 67.17, 2.6.0 67.64, 2.7.2 67.82, so no compiler switch is involved.
- id: i-jtbl-misc5-bbec-converts-alone | keywords: jtbl, rodata, order, midchain, check_bin, misc5, family
  **A TABLE SITTING AT AN 8-ALIGNED OFFSET INSIDE A UNIT RANGE CONVERTS ALONE EVEN WHEN THE TABLES AFTER IT ARE 4 MOD 8 AND STAY ASM** (round 412, `world_map/main/misc5` func_8007BBEC, 217 insns / 868 bytes, `check_bin.sh world_map` prints OK with the C switch live). This corrects the round-396 note on the same unit (`i-jtbl-midchain-4mod8`): misc5's seven tables tile its range at 0x0/0x30/0x50/0x68/0x8C/0x194/0x1AC, and converting BBEC (whose table is the 0x68 one) keeps retail's offsets because the two tables before it belong to the still-asm `func_8007ADD4`, whose `.rodata` is emitted at ITS position in the `.c` file - so the order comes out `[UAS C table 0x0][ADD4 asm 0x30][ADD4 asm 0x50][BBEC C table 0x68][asm 0x8C...]`, exactly retail (lever `i-jtbl-offset-follows-file-position` again; the round-396 "C .rodata precedes asm .rodata" measurement was taken in REPORT mode, where the asm tables are not in the object at all). The rule from round 404 holds: a table is convertible iff its offset in the range is 8-aligned (SUBALIGN(4) in the ld script means the object-section alignment never pads the range start) and everything before it is C-emitted earlier in the file or carved. The 4-mod-8 tables after it (0x8C onward) are still blocked and must stay `INCLUDE_ASM`.
- id: e-narrow-load-vs-shift-then-narrow | keywords: lh, lhu, sll, sra, extension, sign, fixed-point, 16.16, narrow
  **FOR A 16.16 WORD READ AS A SIGNED HALFWORD, `(s16)(((s32 *)p)[0] >> 16)` GIVES RETAIL'S SINGLE `lh` WHERE `((s16 *)p)[1]` GIVES `lhu; sll 16; sra 16`** (round 412, `world_map/main/misc5` func_8007BBEC, 98.80 -> 99.82 at retail's exact 868 bytes / 217 insns). The HImode spelling loads 16 bits and then forces the value through an HImode pseudo, which cc1 2.7.2 extends with a shift pair (2 instructions too many, and it also lets the scheduler hoist the load above the following `lui`); the SImode `>> 16` with a narrowing cast is folded by cc1's shift-into-address rule to `lh 0x2(base)`, exactly what retail has, and it leaves the neighbouring `word >> 16` reads (which cse shares with later full-word uses) as `lw; sra` like retail. Use the shift-then-narrow form whenever the sibling fields of the same record are read with `>> 16`.
- id: m-fold-registers-out-of | keywords: park, stale, diff, register, rename, mnemonic, block-order, big
  **ON A 300+ INSTRUCTION PARK, FOLD THE REGISTER NAMES OUT OF THE TWO LISTINGS BEFORE DECIDING WHAT IS LEFT - the registry of "51 of 82 differing lines are pure renames" is what tells you whether to keep chipping or park.** round 415, slus_006.64/system/sound func_8003C6E8 (359 insns, parked 75.84% / 1440 bytes against retail's 1436): `DIFF_FULL=1 diff_func.sh` reports "246 differing lines", which reads like 246 problems; diffing the two instruction lists with every `$reg` replaced by `R` (and every branch target by `<T>`) shows 51 rename-only lines and 31 real ones, all of which the round-331/350 note had already named. The one size deficit is the PEELED SCAN-LOOP BOTTOM (retail `bnez s1,.L...` into one shared bottom, 5 `slti v0,a2,0x80` in the function; ours inlines a copy, 6) - a reorg artifact of the `do {} while ((cmd = *pScript) >= 0x80)` shape whose removal reorders the blocks more than it fixes. Verdict on that class: the residual is whole-function BLOCK ORDER (gcc emits our post-loop block early and inlines the 0x80/0x81 tails where retail puts them out of line and hoists both constants into `$a3`/`$a1`), so per-instruction spellings cannot converge it; park it with the folded counts instead of briefing another lane of guesses. Tooling fact from the same round: `park.py unpark src/slus_006.64/system/sound.c sound func_8003C6E8` silently does nothing ("no unpark state found") because the script builds the INCLUDE_ASM line as `asm/<arg>`; main-exe units must pass the full `slus_006.64/nonmatchings/system/sound`.
- id: s-same-tu-definition-is-inlined | keywords: inline, jal, call, missing, count, register, t-register, split, tu
  **A CALL THAT RETAIL MAKES WITH `jal` DISAPPEARS IF THE CALLEE IS DEFINED EARLIER IN THE SAME .c - `-O3` inlines it, which also drops every callee-saved save, the frame and the s-registers - so a merged unit that concatenates what were separate retail TUs cannot match such a function and the fix is a unit split, not a spelling.** Measured on `slus_006.64/psyq/libc` `Sprintf` (561 insns, parked 75.75% / 545 insns): retail has `jal memchr`, `jal strlen`, `jal memmove`, while the compile inlines all three (`grep -c jal` on the Sprintf body = 0, the frame drops 0x248 -> 0x220, `regs= 0/0` in the `.frame` comment, and pDst/count/p/len move from s3/s2/s1/s0 to t4/t2/a3/a2). Retail escaped it only because psyq `sprintf.c` was its own TU with prototypes; here memmove/strlen/memchr sit above Sprintf in address order. PROOF the body itself is correct: `tools/scripts/cc1_probe.sh slus_006.64/psyq/libc <same text + extern strlen/memchr/memmove>` emits the three `jal`s, saves ra and s0-s6, and puts the 0xC-byte spec at sp+0x210. A block-scope `extern void* memmove();` inside the body does NOT stop it (measured, still 545 insns). Fix: `config/slus_006.64.yaml` `[0x300E8, c, psyq/libc_sprintf]` + a new `src/slus_006.64/psyq/libc_sprintf.c` with the same PSY-Q flags keyed per file, then un-park.
- id: p-li-live-range-residual-is-2-6-0 | keywords: preset, split, li, live-range, 2.6.0, 2.7.2, dg, mixed, carve
  **A PARK WHOSE ONLY RESIDUAL IS A POST-RELOAD `li`'s PLACE (AND THUS WHICH REGISTER THE DIVISION AROUND IT TAKES) CAN BE A COMPILER VERSION, NOT A SPELLING: measure the 2.6.0 score before writing a BLOCKED note** (round 425, `battle/main/misc11_b` func_80081B58: 99.32 under the unit's 2.7.2, **100.00 under `gcc-2.6.0-psx`**, retail's exact 1356 bytes / 339 insns). Retail's tail is `sra v1,v1,5 / li v0,1 / lui at,%hi(timer) / addu at,at,s0 / sh v1 / j / sb v0,0x2E3(a1)`; 2.7.2 emitted `sra v0 / lui at / addu at / sh v0 / li v0,1 / j / sb v0` - same count, same bytes, 10 differing lines - and `-da` explained it completely: pre-reload the constant IS a real pseudo (`.lreg` insn 753 `(set (reg:QI 283) (const_int 1))`) but it sits AFTER the store insn 748, so greg hands 283 and the quotient pseudo the same `$v0` (disjoint live ranges); retail's 283 is live across 748, which plain C cannot express because cse1 folds an assignment to a local back into the store (measured: a `u8 spent` temp between the division and the store compiles byte-identically to the inline form, and `gaugeSpent = 1;` above the store is 97.32 / 1368 because the `sb` leaves the `j` delay slot). So the lesson is ORDER OF ATTACK: `try_flags.sh <unit> <Func> --gcc gcc-2.6.0-psx` (one second) before a `-da`/permuter investigation of a `li`-position residual - here it was the whole answer after two permuter runs (base=90 best=90 both) had said the body was stuck. Do NOT switch the unit on that score alone: the same screen `--all --gcc gcc-2.6.0-psx` keeps `BattleInitTargetSlotFlags` 100.00 but drops `BattleAppendCommandRecord` 100.00 -> 99.91, so this unit is mixed and needs a one-function carve-out (`tools/scripts/split_unit.py`, then a per-file gears.toml key on gcc-2.6.0-psx).
- id: a-carve-out-needs-its-own-rodata-range-and-its-callee-prototypes | keywords: split, carve-out, preset, rodata, jtbl, prototype, mask, andi, jal, slot
  **EXECUTED: a one-function carve-out onto gcc 2.6.0 closes `battle/main/misc11_b`'s func_80081B58 at retail's exact 1356 bytes / 339 insns (round 438), but `split_unit.py` leaves TWO things to do by hand, and each one alone fails `make check`** - so budget both before starting. (1) The target unit's `.rodata` tables must travel with the new TU: config/battle.yaml gets its own `.rodata` subsegment at the first table offset (misc11_b's range became `[0x520, .rodata, main/misc11_b]` + `[0x5A0, .rodata, main/misc11_b_r2]`, the head's four 0x20-byte tables and the tail's three), because the generated linker script places an object with no `.rodata` subsegment far later in the block - the first failure was exactly 0x5A0, `g_StrActorNoFormat 1759 bytes` in culprit.sh being the shifted-string symptom. (2) A carved-out function LOSES its callee's prototypes (the callee was defined in the old TU, so the header never declared it), and an implicit `void f()` declaration drops the `andi a0,sN,0xff` the `u8` parameter masks: func_80081B58's `BattleAppendCommandRecord(arg0)` came out `move a0,s1` in the `jal` delay slot against retail's `andi a0,s1,0xff`, a 4-byte diff with everything else identical - add `extern void BattleAppendCommandRecord(u8 arg0);` to the new TU. Also: `split_unit.py` (round 425, twice) copied EVERY C function into every new file; check `wc -l` and the per-file function list before building and fix by hand, and pass function NAMES to `--to` (its docstring shows an address, which the code silently ignores, printing "1 run"). Wart worth knowing: `try_flags.sh battle/main/misc11_b <Func> --gcc gcc-2.6.0-psx` prints `compile-failed` for this unit although the identical cpp+cc1+maspsx command line run by hand succeeds, so confirm a 2.6.0 screen by compiling the unit's .i yourself before believing it.
- id: e-narrow-local-for-a-byte-arg | keywords: u16, u8, narrow, local, register, copy, andi, defer, call, argument, s32
  **A LOCAL THAT HOLDS A BYTE-DERIVED VALUE AND IS PASSED TO A `u8` PARAMETER CAN DECIDE BOTH ITS REGISTER AND WHETHER THE CALL SITE COPIES: declare it `u16`, not `s32`** (round 427, `battle/main/misc13` func_80087AF0, 99.80 -> 99.96 at retail's exact 1004 bytes / 251 insns, then `check_bin.sh battle` OK). The arm is `step = *(volatile u8 *)&g_BattleCommandQueue[0x2DC] - 8;` passed to `BattleMaskHasStatusBit(u16, u8)`; retail keeps the byte in `$a1` and masks it IN PLACE (`andi a1,a1,0xff` in the `jal` delay slot) with the `g_BattleActorCharIds` chain in `$v0`, while the `s32 step` body mirrors the pair (`lbu v0`, `andi a1,v0,0xff`). `u16 step;` moved both into retail's registers with no other edit (the permuter found it: base=130 best=10 over 1498 iterations). Note the variable must ALSO be distinct from any later use of the same value: reusing one `s32 stepNo` for this arm and for a later `BattleScriptScanForOpcode62` result forces one pseudo across both, which is what produced the copy in the first place.
- id: c-double-negation-on-a-slot-stride-from-a-pointer-global | keywords: addu, operand, base-first, index-first, negate, double, slot, stride, pointer, global, register, mirror
  **THE DOUBLE-NEGATED OFFSET ALSO FLIPS A `slot * STRIDE` SUM WHOSE BASE IS A POINTER GLOBAL LOADED FROM MEMORY, NOT ONLY A LOOP INDEX OR A CONSTANT-ADDRESS MEM BASE - three mirrored sites in one function closed with one edit each** (round 427, `battle/main/misc13` func_80087AF0: `(g_BattleCommandQueue - -((u8)arg0 * 0x40))[0x3C]` and `(g_BattleCommandQueue - -(slot * 0x40))[0x3C]`, 99.60 -> 99.68 -> 99.76 -> 99.80). The plain subscript `g_BattleCommandQueue[(u8)arg0 * 0x40 + 0x3C]` and the embedded-assignment form `(pQueueRow = g_BattleCommandQueue + slot * 0x40)[0x3C]` both give index-first (`sll v0,a0,6` / `lw v1,queue` / `addu v0,v0,v1`); retail has the base in `$v0` and the scaled slot in `$v1`. This is the same cc1 behaviour as lever `c-a-double-negated-offset-flips`, so try it per site before parking a register mirror - it is semantically inert and one line.
- id: k-g8-folds-a-pointer-local-g0-keeps-it | keywords: la, addiu, at, pointer, local, g0, g8, delay, slot
  **A POINTER LOCAL TO A SCALAR GLOBAL IS FOLDED BACK TO THE BARE SYMBOL UNDER -G8 AND KEPT IN A REGISTER UNDER -G0, WHICH IS THE DIFFERENCE BETWEEN THE ASSEMBLER'S `lui $at / sw %lo($at)` STORE AND RETAIL'S `la / sw 0(reg)` IN A BRANCH DELAY SLOT** (round 428, world_map/main/misc5 func_8007ADD4, 82.67% under the unit's XenoWorldMapDiv -G8, 86.32% for the SAME body under `--gp -G0`; try_flags `--gp -G0` is the one-command probe). Retail keeps `&g_WorldMapCameraTargetZ` in `$a2` (lui+addiu, then `lw 0(a2)` twice with `sw 0(a2)` in the beqz delay slot) and `&g_WorldMapViewRotationY` in `$a3` for a `lhu 0(a3)/addiu/sh 0(a3)` read-modify-write, while every other global in the same function uses the `$at` macro form (`lui $at,D_/sh %lo($at)`, and loads as `lui $v1,%hi; lhu $v1,%lo($v1)`). Writing `pTargetZ = &g_WorldMapCameraTargetZ; *pTargetZ += 0x3A000; if (*pTargetZ > N)` reproduces the `$a2` form exactly under -G0 and folds to `lui $v0 / lw 0(v0)` plus a `$at` store, a `nop` beqz delay slot and 2 surplus instructions, under -G8. So on a diff whose only surplus is a symbol address that retail holds in a general register (and whose other accesses use `$at`), do NOT conclude cdk/-G0 for the unit from one function: screen `try_flags.sh <unit> --all --gp -G0` first - here four already-matched functions break (func_8007C724 100 -> 27.96, WorldMapUpdateActorState 99.95, func_8007BBEC 99.82, WorldMapUpdateGroup4Actor 99.94), so it is a unit SPLIT (per-file preset), not a switch, and report PRESET_G0 with both scores.
- id: c-a-switch-shared-tail-sits-at-a-label | keywords: switch, tail, shared, merge, crossjump, block, order, label, goto, delay, slot, reorg
  **A TAIL SHARED BY TWO SWITCH CASES IS SOURCE-LEVEL (a `goto` label between the case bodies), NOT A CROSS-JUMP MERGE, AND reorg CANNOT HOIST AN INSTRUCTION OUT OF A BLOCK WITH TWO PREDECESSORS - which is why retail shows the same `li` in BOTH arms' delay slots** (round 432, slus_006.64/psyq/libgpu_b func_80045C94, parked 93.30% with a 91.36% variant at retail's exact 176 bytes). gcc 2.7.2 has no -freorder-blocks, so blocks come out in source order: the tail written once AFTER the switch lands after the default (72.48), duplicated in both arms cross-jump merges it at the SECOND arm's position and the first arm pays a `j`+nop (85.91), and only a `case 1: ... goto tw; tw: <tail> case 2: ... goto tw;` label placed between the two case bodies gives retail's `[dispatch][case1][tail][case2][non-reverse][default]`. With the tail in one block at the label, reorg fills the arms' delay slots from their own fall-through blocks instead of with the tail's head instruction (`li v0,1024`), because `fill_simple_delay_slots` only steals from a single-predecessor thread; putting the constant in each arm wins that slot but cse then merges the two loads and the shared value lands in a caller-saved $a1 instead of $v0. So expect a both-ways choice: exact block layout + one displaced `li`, or both delay slots right and one instruction short. The same lever's earlier entries are `i-a-cross-jumped-block-keeps` (round 421, goto label closes a switch park) and `d-crossjump-survivor-is-the-last`.

Round 451 closed a park of this exact family with the label pair, on a plain (non-shared-body) switch and with no `do`-block involved, which is the cheapest form of the lever to try first (world_map/main/misc11 func_8008C364, XenoWorldMap, 95.87/460 -> 99.87/460 at retail's own byte count; the last 4 bytes are a 4-mod-8 jtbl pad, a separate problem). The switch had two *different* shared things: a constant block (`result = 3;` alone, reached from four even cases at the table AND from case 1's failure) and a four-store tail (reached from both of those arms). Written as duplicated bodies, cross-jumping merged the constant block into the SECOND copy's position - inside case 1, right after its `bne` - for 95.87. Writing them as `case 0: case 2: case 4: case 6: default: setResultThree: result = 3; goto setZero;` plus `case 1: if (gearId == GEAR_ID_NONE) goto setResultThree; <body>` and a `setZero:` label above the four stores reproduces retail's `[dispatch][result = 3][case 1][tail][case 3][case 5]` block for block, with case 1's `beq` going backwards into the constant block. Two details matter: the tail must be a LABEL, not duplicated (a duplicated tail still merges); and each arm that shares the constant block must reach it with a `goto`, because an arm that carries its own `result = 3` is what gets merged away. Same round, same unit: the group whose members the table shares is read off the table's WORD TARGETS, not off the case semantics - `case 3:` and `case 7:` grouped together measured 95.87 with 8 words the table does not have (retail's entry 7 points at case 5's body).
Round 433 added a measured lever here (battling/main/misc3 func_80077A9C): an empty `do { } while (0);` between a flag-word read-modify-write and a dependency-free constant set (`flag = 1;`) pins the `li` BELOW the store, which sched1 otherwise hoists to the top of the basic block. The A/B that proves it: the same `.i` compiled with `-fno-schedule-insns` already puts the `li` in retail's slot, so the C's RTL order is right and the residual is sched1, not source order (three other statement positions all measured 99.49 with the `li` second). With the empty loop the body is 99.95% (retail's exact 1720 bytes, every instruction identical, the only objdiff delta being the jtbl vs .rodata operand) and `check_bin.sh battling` prints OK. So before parking a park whose note blames "sched1 hoists a free constant", try the empty loop as a scheduling boundary first: this is the same mechanism as h-an-empty-do-while-pins-a-run-of-stores, read in the other direction.

Round 448 measured the OTHER shape of the same boundary and it is not interchangeable (battle/main/misc11_b_r2 func_800826CC, Default 2.7.2 -O2 -G8, 96.00/340 -> 100.00/340 MATCH). The residual was one block position: retail stores `g_BattleSlotFlags[arg0 * 8] = 2` BEFORE the `lbu` of the `if` condition, our compile sank the store after the load (85 vs 85 insns, 340 bytes, every other instruction identical). A standalone empty `do { } while (0);` between the store and the `if` pins the position (98.35) but costs 4 bytes: the constant then lands in `$a3` instead of retail's `$a0` and gcc pre-copies `arg0` into `$a0` for the condition's index, so the join's `move a0,s0` is emitted twice. Wrapping the whole `if` in the loop instead - `do { if (...) {...} } while (0);` - is byte-identical to retail's 340: the store stays outside the loop note and the load inside it, and the register shape (constant in `a0` live across the arm, one `move a0,s0` at the join) is untouched. So for a diff that is ONE displaced pair of memory ops against a following `if`, wrap the `if` rather than inserting a bare barrier, and if the percentage rises while the byte count does not, the barrier moved a register and the fix is the wrapper form.
- id: c-two-scalar-sums-in-one-arm-can-want-opposite-orders | keywords: addu, operand, base-first, index-first, scalar, sum, cse, reassociate
  **TWO SCALAR SUMS IN THE SAME ARM CAN WANT OPPOSITE OPERAND ORDERS, AND SPELLING ONE THE WRONG WAY MAKES cc1 CSE IT WITH ITS NEIGHBOUR (`+1` folded into the neighbour's constant)** (round 434, battle/main/misc2 func_80075938, parked 90.61 -> 92.72%, gcc 2.6.0). Retail's gauge quad wants `x0 = origin + slot * 0x60 + 0x2D` (`addu v0,v0,t3`, origin first, then `addiu 0x2D`) but `x1 = slot * 0x60 + (origin + 0x2C) + barWidth + 1` (`addiu 0x2C`, then `addu v0,t3,v0`, t3 first). Writing BOTH slot-first gives `addu v0,t3,v0` at x0/x2 (90.61); writing x1 slot-first while x0 is origin-first is what keeps them apart - with both origin-first cc1 folds x1 into the x0 subexpression plus `barWidth` (`addu v0,v0,t3` then `addiu v0,v0,0x2D` then `addu v0,v0,a0`), i.e. it reassociates `(o + t3 + 0x2C) + (w + 1)` into `(o + t3 + 0x2D) + w`. The two sums share the `origin + slot * 0x60` pair, so the lesson is per-site: match the opcode in the `.s`, not the algebra.
- id: p-halfword-store-copy-means-260 | keywords: copy, move, halfword, store, subreg, 2.6.0, split, park
  **"RETAIL HAS `move vN,sM` BEFORE A HALFWORD STORE WHERE OUR BODY STORES `sh sM`" IS A gcc 2.6.0 SIGNATURE, NOT A SOURCE CEILING: 2.6.0 materialises the truncated subreg in a fresh register, 2.7.2 coalesces it, and a `cc1_probe.sh` sweep of every truncation spelling on the 2.7.2 flags folds them all** (round 434, battling/main/misc12 func_80089330: 97.91% / 508 bytes / 127 insns under the unit's XenoBattlingNoGp 2.7.2 -G0 against retail's 516 / 129, and an objdump diff of a hand-built gcc-2.6.0 -G0 object against `expected/asm/battling/main/misc12.s.o` is instruction-for-instruction identical - registers, frame, branch targets - with the only residual the tile stores naming `D_8009A1C0` with addends 3/4/8/A/C/E where retail relocs `D_8009A1C3/1C4/1C8/1CA/1CC/1CE`, the linked-bytes-identical case). Measured on the same body: 2.7.2-cdk does not compile the unit; 2.6.0 at -G4/-G8 score the same 99.53 but drop `BattleSetFlag10000` to 44.86, so -G0 stays; the untyped spelling (separate `D_8009A1C3/1C4/1C8/1CA/1CC/1CE` externs instead of the typed `BattleTilePacket`) is 81.40 under 2.6.0 and does not compile under 2.7.2. The unit cannot switch wholesale (under 2.6.0 `BattleSetMatrixFromPoints` 100.00 -> 91.63 with real `lw`->`lhu` differences and `BattleBuildScaledMatrixFromVectors` 100.00 -> 94.71), so the fix is a one-function carve-out: `[0x19840, 0x19A44)` under 2.6.0 inside misc12's `[0x18E50, 0x1A26C)` range. Screen a unit with `try_flags.sh <unit> --all --gcc gcc-2.6.0-psx` and read real differences from an objdump diff against `expected/`, because objdiff's reloc rows make a byte-identical body read 99.x.
- id: a-block-scoped-copy-of-a-shared-pointer-fixes-the-hand-out | keywords: regalloc, callee-saved, block-local, shared, rotation, base, switch, matrix
  **WHEN SEVERAL ARMS OR GROUPS ASSIGN THE SAME POINTER VARIABLE, GIVE EACH ITS OWN BLOCK-SCOPED COPY (`case 0: { SVECTOR *pVec = ...; }`, `{ WorldMapEntry *pMat = ...; }`) - one function-level variable merges the definitions into a single long-lived pseudo and rotates the whole callee-saved hand-out, and the block-local copies land exactly on retail's** (round 434, world_map/main/misc4 func_80079778, 94.77 -> 95.80 -> 99.31 at retail's exact 2292 bytes / 573 insns, XenoWorldMap). At 94.77 the three switch arms that compute `pScratch + 0xA0` gave one pseudo live across the whole switch, so the hand-out came out `$s0`=g_WorldMapGroups, `$s2`=scratch, `$s3`=pVec against retail's `$s0`=pVec, `$s2`=g_WorldMapGroups, `$s3`=scratch, and each arm also lost the `addiu s0,s3,0xA0` that retail re-forms per arm; declaring `pVec` inside each arm's brace block restored retail's 3 addius, retail's arm-head order (`li a0,11 / lw / addiu`) and put g_WorldMapGroups in `$s2` and the scratch base in `$s3` (95.80). The same edit on the tail's four matrix-distribution groups (`pMat` re-assigned before each group -> one block-local pointer per group) moved the 8-word copies' base from `$a2` back to `$v0` with the temporaries in `$v1,$a0,$a1,$a2` and closed 215 diff lines at once (95.80 -> 99.31). So a park note that blames "local declaration order does not move the callee-saved rotation" is usually a shared variable, not the allocator: split it per block first.

- id: m-a-stale-notes-residual-diagnosis | keywords: park, stale, note, frame, spill, slot, hoist, residual
  **A STALE PARK'S RESIDUAL DIAGNOSIS IS AS FICTION AS ITS PERCENTAGE: read the `.frame` line's `vars=` and the slot SPACING before believing a note that explains a frame gap as "N unnamed locals"** (round 435, menu/main/misc3 func_801CBD90, 594 insns, re-parked at 78.17% / 2316 bytes / 579 insns on its first honest measurement - the parked body had never compiled, an undeclared `ownCard` where retail passes the destination card and a duplicated `slot` local). The note called retail's 0x100-vs-0xE8 gap "six 4-byte locals this body does not name"; retail's `.frame` is vars=200 (0xC8 = two 0x40 buffers + a 6-byte prefix + a 2-byte digit + pad + SIX slots) and those six slots at 0xA8..0xD0 are 8 bytes apart, i.e. the SPILL signature from j-slot-spacing - retail spilled arg0, repeat, fileFd, ready, result and chunkIndex, so the hunt the note prescribed could never succeed. The body's vars=176 with four spills at 0xA0..0xB8 came from hoist ranking instead: retail keeps `&path` in `$s7` and MENU_SLOT_NONE (0xFF) in `$fp`, the body kept chunkIndex's 0xFF in `$s7` and `1` in `$fp`, and every sp offset plus every s-register name in the remaining 453 diff lines follows from that one tie-break - see g-hoisted-loop-invariant-constant, and note that spelling the sentinel as its own source local is byte-identical because gcc unifies the two `const_int` 0xFF pseudos. Also measured there and dead: gcc-2.7.2 gives 78.09 and 2.7.2-cdk does not compile the body, so a preset screen costs one command and closes that door. Verify the CALL SET while you are in the note: this one's 62 `jal` symbols matched retail's as a multiset (block order only), which is what makes "the gap is spill code, not a missing block" a measurement rather than a hope.
Round 443 measured the LIMIT of the lever above: **it does nothing when the labeled block is post-reload register-IDENTICAL to the duplicate copy that survives** - cross_jump then merges the label's block into that copy exactly as it merged the arms, so no block moves (gear_shop_menu/main/render func_801D2B74, 633 insns: the armor/frame `equipFlags`/`price = value` tail written once at a `goto equipGearPart` label between the FRAME and ENGINE case bodies is 2508 bytes, the same as the three duplicated tails; retail's shape - armor `j`s into a tail after the FRAME arm, the ENGINE arm keeps its own copy - needs the engine's `lw`/`sw` to be in registers the shared block does not use, which is an allocator tie at that site, not a source order). Check for that before spending a lane on the label: dump your own `.s` and grep the shared block's instructions - if the surviving copy has the same register set as the block you are about to put at a label, the label cannot change the merge target.

## psyq printf-family varargs

- id: psyp-address-taken-va-list | keywords: va_list, va_arg, varargs, printf, memory, spill, stdarg, address
  **A va_list THAT IS A PLAIN `va_list ap` LOCAL IS KEPT IN A REGISTER, BUT READING IT AS `va_arg(*((va_list*)&ap), T)` MAKES IT AN ADDRESS-TAKEN MEMORY OBJECT AND REPRODUCES RETAIL'S 5-INSTRUCTION `lw v1,0x220(sp); nop; addiu v0,v1,4; sw v0,0x220(sp); lw a0,0(v1)` PER ARGUMENT** (round 435, slus_006.64/psyq/libc_sprintf Sprintf, 90.12 -> 96.60%). With the register form the whole body loses three instructions per `va_arg` (nine sites), gcc needs a ninth saved register (`&va_area` in `$s7`) and every register in the function renumbers; with the address-taken form `ap` lands in its own stack slot, the three format constants take `$s4-$s6` and `count`/`pDst`/`p` land in `$s2`/`$s3`/`$s1` exactly as retail. `volatile va_list ap` also forces memory but costs 39 extra instructions (88.86%), so use the address-of spelling. Same trick moves a `va_list` you pass to a static helper that -O3 inlines.
- id: d-combine-folds-through-any-intervening-statement | keywords: combine, fold, shift, srl, separations, intervening, block, coalesce, return
  **gcc 2.6.0's combine FOLDS `(x >> a) >> b` INTO ONE `x >> (a+b)` NO MATTER WHAT SITS BETWEEN THE TWO SHIFTS, so no inserted statement, copy local or reordered assignment can stop it - only a different BASIC BLOCK or an untracked value can, and neither is spellable from the C when retail's two shifts are adjacent in its own asm** (round 437, slus_006.64/system/sound func_8003B644, parked 96.74% at retail's exact 748 bytes / 187 insns, XenoLibrary = gcc-2.6.0). Retail is `mflo v0 / srl s8,v0,0x7 / srl v0,s8,0xf / andi v0,v0,0x1` (the clamp test reads the volume VARIABLE) against every body's `srl v0,v0,0x16`, and the intervening-statement measurement is the new part: moving `elementFlags = element >> 8;` up between the scaling and the clamp does NOT block the fold (it only hoists the `srl t0,s0,8` above the `mult`, 16 differing lines, 95.03), and a `u32 clampTest = volume;` copy between them is deleted by flow and folds anyway. So when a park note says the fold survived "declaration order, source order and a do/while", believe it: the fold is a combine equivalence on the shift's destination, not an adjacency effect. Presets on that body: cdk 94.57, 2.7.2 94.72 against 2.6.0's 96.74.
  Companion measurement in the same unit (func_8003E360, parked 95.31%, one instruction SHORT at 31 insns against retail's 32): a returned incremented pointer keeps retail's `addiu sN,sN,1` + `addu v0,sN,zero` ONLY when the increment's OLD value is used - the matched SoundScriptSetVolume next door gets that pair from `*pScript++` inside an expression - while `pScript++; return pScript;`, `return ++pScript;`, `pScript += 1; return pScript;` and `return pScript += 1;` all coalesce to a single `addiu v0,s1,1` (flow copy-prop, four spellings measured, 95.31 each). Moving the increment up so its old value is needed (`index = *pScript++;`) does produce the two instructions but makes the pointer live across the call and rotates the whole s-block (33 insns / 132 bytes / 84.16%).
  Round 450 addendum on func_8003B644 (same 96.74% / 748 bytes / 187 insns, still parked): the fold is NOT register-neutral, which is why the other three clusters of that park must not be reordered on their own. Retail's `srl s8,v0,0x7 / srl v0,s8,0xf` KILLS the `mflo` result in v0 at the first shift; the folded form `srl v0,v0,0x16` drags v0 four bytes further, and v0's longer live range is exactly what competes for the park's remaining differences (the `lhu t0,24(sp)` pan reload and `addiu a0,s1,96` for `&pElement->voice_data` both land later in retail, and retail has `lbu v0,22(s2)` before `sw s7,5(s0)`). So treat those as DOWNSTREAM of the fold, not as statement-order work: -O1 is 67.76 and -O3 compile-fails (cc1 rejects) on the body, leaving -O2 as the only level.
- id: e-narrow-local-first-in-each-arm-keeps-the-raw-sum | keywords: move, copy, cse, u8, narrow, truncation, parameter, arm, sum, prologue
  **A PARKED FUNCTION ONE INSTRUCTION SHORT OF RETAIL'S EXACT SIZE, WITH AN EXTRA PROLOGUE `move tN,a1` COPY AND THE WHOLE `addu` SUM FOR A u8 PARAMETER DISPLACED TO THE CALL, IS THE *TYPE* OF THE LOCAL THE SUM IS ASSIGNED TO - AND IT HAS TO BE THE FIRST STATEMENT OF EVERY ARM** (round 441, battle/main/misc15 func_8008B224, 96.54 -> MATCH at retail's exact 596 bytes / 149 insns, XenoBattle260 = gcc 2.6.0). Retail computes TWO sums per arm from one `sll arg2,1`: the call's argument on the RAW parameter in place (`addu a1,a1,v0`, then `andi a1,a1,0xff` in the jal slot) and the row offset on the zero-extended copy (`andi v1,v1,0xff` / `addu v0,v0,v1`, the copy itself sitting in the branch's delay slot). `bit = arg1 + arg2 * 2;` with a **`s32`/`u32` local** forces the zero-extension into the sum, so cse merges it with the offset's and the body is 8 bytes SHORT (584-588); with an **`u8` local** the sum is a QImode-truncating one, cc1 leaves the operands raw and the two sums stay distinct. Placement is the other half: with the assignment after the entry/count loads the sum sinks to the call site and the parameter has to be preserved in `$t0` from the prologue; with the assignment as the arm's FIRST statement, cc1 emits it in retail's position. Both arms need it - one arm with a u8 local and the other without put the surviving copy back in the prologue (150 insns / 600 bytes, 97.45). Read this before re-parking an arm whose diff is a displaced `addu` plus a prologue `move`.
- id: a-in-place-dest-may-be-a-second-pseudo | keywords: regalloc, in-place, sra, sll, compound, merge, root, source, counter-example
  **A RESULT THAT LANDS IN THE SAME REGISTER AS ITS DYING SOURCE IS NOT ALWAYS AN IN-PLACE C UPDATE: two pseudos with disjoint lives share a callee-saved register, and spelling them as one variable with `/= 2` / `*= 2` MERGES the pseudos and LOSES instructions** (round 447, battling/main/misc3 func_80075B50, parked 96.42% at retail's exact 2124 bytes / 528 insns, Default preset). Retail has `addu v0,s0,v0 / sra s0,v0,0x1` for `amount = angle / 2` and `sll s0,s0,0x1` for `amount = angle * 2`, which reads exactly like the round-437 `x = x >> 8` in-place family; folding `angle` into `amount` with compound assignments measures 95.41% / 2112 bytes / 525 insns (three instructions GONE, so the sizes alone refute it), i.e. the original had two variables that the allocator gave one register because `angle` dies at the division. Read the size before porting an in-place spelling. Same body, same round: splitting a variable that two sequential loops reuse (`amount` -> a per-loop `slotAmount`) is BYTE-IDENTICAL at 96.42 - a per-loop split only moves a hand-out when the register is contested by an OVERLAPPING live range, otherwise gcc reuses the register for both pseudos and nothing changes; and the whole residual there is a three-way pinned hand-out (pointer/offset/amount rotated among `$s0/$s2/$s4`, `-da` processing order `... 74 ... 73 86 168 77 ...` with 74 unable to take `$s0` because its conflict set already lists 16).

- id: d-a-symbol-address-live-across-a-call-is-rematerialised-not-shared | keywords: remat, address, callee-saved, call, pseudo, fold, return, constant
  **A SYMBOL ADDRESS USED AS A CALL ARGUMENT AND AGAIN AFTER THE CALL IS REMATERIALISED INTO BOTH REGISTERS, never shared in one callee-saved register, so a retail sequence `lui s0 / addiu s0 / move a2,s0 (jal slot) / lbu v1,0(s0)` is unreachable from any C in gcc 2.6.0** — and the twin fold: a local's constant is propagated into `return local` inside a STRAIGHT-LINE block but NOT when the return sits at a merge label, so a park one instruction short with a missing `move s0,zero` (store) beside a returned copy is a cse fold, not a source shape. Measured round 450, menu/main/misc12 func_801E93A0 (98.39% / 760 bytes / 190 insns against retail 772 / 193): spelling the 0x15 query's status pointer as the literal `&g_MenuCdStatus` at both uses gives 98.23 / 768 / 192 with `lui a2; addiu a2; ... lui v1; lbu v1,0(v1)` (fresh `u_char* pStatus = &g_MenuCdStatus;` measures identically), where reusing the already-live pointer local gives no remat at all; and moving the success path's `result = 0;` to after its two calls is byte-identical, i.e. position inside the block is not the lever. Both residuals are needed for MATCH, so neither alone can close such a park.

## struct / address folding (appended out of order by round 447)

symptoms: cast view struct hoists a global base, per-load %lo addends, two locals swapped in s0/s1
try: cast view struct array member fields regalloc swapped
- id: a-cast-view-struct-hoists-the-array-base | keywords: struct, cast, array, member, cse, hoist, lui, addend, regalloc
  **A CAST VIEW STRUCT OVER A STRUCT MEMBER ARRAY (`((View *)g_State.rows)[id].unk4`) MAKES gcc CSE `&g_State+0x16BC` INTO A REGISTER, WHILE THE HEADER'S OWN NAMED FIELDS (`g_State.rows[id].commandMask`) KEEP RETAIL'S PER-LOAD `lui $at,%hi(g_State)` + `addu` + `lhu %lo+0x16C0/0x16C2` - the two loads carry different %lo addends so retail's never CSE, and the cast ruins that** (round 447, battle/main/misc1 func_80070F40, XenoBattle260; that change alone took the park 96.03 -> 97.05% and removed 28 diff lines). Before writing a view struct for sign/wideness, check whether the header already types the field the way the access needs: here `commandMask`/`characterConditionMask` were u16 all along, so the "unsigned view" existed only because an old note said the header was s16. Companion find on the same body, worth 97.24 -> 97.52% and 32 diff lines: two heap blocks used at two different sites want their OWN locals (`skinBlock1/skinBlock2` for the first pair, `block1/block2` for the later D_800D2D3C/D_800D2F60 pair, the first pair declared first) - sharing one pair made gcc hand them out mirrored ($s1/$s0 against retail's $s0/$s1) and added a `move s1,v0` copy in the later site.

  **Round 448 re-measured the same body (unparked, live: 67.82% / 3700 bytes / 925 insns against retail's 3708 / 927) and closed the last two source-side doors on it, so a later lane should not re-unpark it.** Retail's saved set is s0-s6+ra and its `$s6` is `frames` (the `addiu s6,zero,1` at 0x517F8 is the `if (frames == 0) frames = 1` clamp, NOT a hoisted constant), so the single surplus register really is only the const-1 group - there is no second hoisted value to remove. And retail's 0x9D arm stores element `[0]` inline in EACH branch (`sh v1,%lo(A)(v0)` off the `lui`'s hi word, `addiu v0,v0,%lo(A)` in the `j`'s delay slot) and cross-jumps only `[1]`,`[2]`, where the compile cross-jumps all three off a callee-saved base; that arm's missing 2 insns are not source-order reachable because in cdk `-fschedule-insns` the delay slot after the `bne` holds the OTHER symbol's `lui`, i.e. the branch bodies are already reordered by the scheduler. Its `g_BattleSlotVectors` is an `SVECTOR`, so the typed spelling (`pSlotVec->vx = ...`) is available but changes no byte: the offsets are the same.
